# Style guide: Sinnoh Diorama

The art direction chosen in plan 04 · G1 (2026-10-01). Every graphics session follows these rules; when a rule has to change, change it here first.

## The direction in one paragraph

Three layers, each with one job:

| Layer | Style | Code |
|---|---|---|
| **Overworld** | HD-2D: pixel-art ground, buildings and characters inside a lit 3D diorama, with strong tilt-shift depth of field, bloom and warm grading | `WorldRenderer`, `PixelGround`, `CharacterSprites` |
| **Battles** | Full 3D: smooth cel-shaded Pokémon and trainers on a modelled stage with soft foliage, a painted sky and clean filtered textures | `BattleRenderer`, `SoftFoliage`, `SkyPainter` |
| **Interface** | Crisp vector UI (Nunito, anti-aliased panels) in Platinum's colour language; Pokémon appear as **2D pixel sprites**, as in the main games | `ModernUi`, `UI/Kit` |

The layers are allowed to differ in rendering technique, never in palette, light direction or mood. What ties them together: the same greens, sands and sky blues; light always from the upper left; the same time of day in the field and in battle; the same interface on top of both; and (G9) a battle intro transition that carries the player from the diorama into the 3D stage.

The numbers in this guide live in `ArtLook` as one light rig per layer and time of day.

## Reference frames

`dotnet run --project tools/ShotHarness -- <dir> look [before dir]` renders the reference frames by day (Twinleaf, dialogue, battle, move menu, party, Route 201, Player's house, Lake Verity, options). Given the folder of an earlier run, it also writes `compare_*.png` before/after boards. `times` renders Twinleaf, Sandgem and a battle at each time of day, then each quality preset; `terrain` renders water, grass, trees and ground kinds; `buildings` renders every building from the street by day and after dark, and every room. Graphics sessions start with a `look` run (the "before") and end with the boards.

## Rules for every layer

- **No single-pixel noise.** Texture detail comes from placed shapes and clusters, never from per-texel random values. The field ground is tested: under 1.5 % of its texels may differ from all eight neighbours (`StyleGuideTests`). The old ground was about 30 %.
- **Light from the upper left**, sun slightly toward the camera, so faces and fronts are lit and shadows fall to the upper right.
- **Shade with colour, not black.** Shadows lean violet-blue, highlights lean warm; outlines are a darker shade of the surface they surround.
- **One pixel size per layer.** Pixel art is shown at one texel density and whole-number scales only, with point filtering; smooth art is mipmapped and filtered. Never mix the two inside one layer.
- **Readable at a glance.** Silhouettes before detail: if a detail doesn't read from the game camera, leave it out.

## Overworld (HD-2D)

### Pixel grid

- **32 texels per world unit** (one tile) for everything: ground (`GroundBaker.ArtTile`), buildings and props (each face painted at its exact size into the map's `ArtSheet`), character sprites (`CharacterSprites.TexelsPerUnit`). With the field camera that is about 3 layout units per texel, 6 real pixels at 4K.
- Point filtering everywhere; no mipmaps on pixel art.
- Upright things (walls, sprites, signs) are stretched by `VS = 1 / cos(pitch)` so they read at true proportions from Platinum's steep camera (pitch 59.05°, FOV 16.18°).
- **Upright things stand upright on screen.** Under that camera, perspective would make everything tall lean outward, by up to 20° at the sides of the screen, and shear its pixel art. Outdoors the vertex shader takes the lean out (`ArtLook.FieldUpright`, `FieldShaders.SetUpright`): every vertex is drawn straight above the point on the ground beneath it. A wall is then an undistorted rectangle, a sprite keeps square texels, and the ground keeps its perspective. Rooms keep true perspective, because their side walls are what makes them a doll's house.
- **A room is seen whole; a hall is followed.** The camera looks at a room's middle and pulls back as the room deepens, up to the depth of a fourteen-tile room. A hall deeper than that (a Gym) keeps that distance and follows the player along its length, between its back wall and its door, so its people stay the size they are in any room. A hall wider than the picture (more than thirty tiles of floor across: the floors of Team Galactic's headquarters) is followed across its width the same way, between its side walls, and its shadows with it.

### Ground

Baked per map by `PixelGround`: soft tile masks thresholded into hard pixel edges, then placed details.

| Element | Colour | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| Lawn | `104,190,98` | flat base |
| Lawn patches | `120,200,102` | clean-edged blobs a few tiles wide, one step lighter |
| Tuft marks | `70,150,82`, tip `160,222,122` | three-blade "v", 2–3 per tile on a jittered grid |
| Forest floor | `58,126,82` | under the tree margin |
| Tall-grass ground | `48,124,70` | |
| Path | `222,204,160` | rounded corners, never pixel fringes |
| Path rim / inner light | `166,140,104` / `236,222,186` | one texel each; the light line on the north and west sides |
| Pebbles | `168,160,150`, light `208,202,192`, shadow `214,186,132` | 2×2 with a highlight texel, at most one cluster per tile |
| Pond stones / sea sand | `168,160,150` / `238,224,172` | band around water |
| Contact shade by walls | two flat steps, 14 % and 28 % toward `30,70,50` | never a smooth gradient |

Other ground kinds follow the lawn's recipe (flat base, clean-edged patches one step lighter, a one-texel rim where they meet another ground, placed marks of two texels or more):

| Kind | Base / patches / rim | Marks |
|---|---|---|
| Sand | `238,224,172` / `246,236,196` / `206,188,138` | short ripple arcs `214,196,140` |
| Dirt | `176,136,96` / `194,156,112` / `138,102,70` | clods: 2×2 `146,108,74` with a light texel |
| Snow | `186,204,232` / `196,212,238` / `150,172,214` | drift lines `168,188,224`, three texels long (kept well below white, so the patches of it in Twinleaf Town stay snow under the day light instead of blooming to a blank; snow country grades the light colder still, "Snow" in the areas' table) |
| Cave floor | `112,100,104` / `130,118,120` / `74,66,80` | cracks `82,72,84`, pebbles `150,140,140` |
| Rock | `158,152,150` / `176,170,166` / `112,106,116` | cracks: bent lines `104,100,112` three to five texels long, each with a light chip `208,204,200` beside it |
| Ice | `150,200,236` / `172,214,242` / `112,166,216` | glints: diagonal strokes `232,246,255`, three texels (blue enough to stay ice under the day light, like the snow) |
| Marsh | `124,106,86` / `140,122,98` / `90,76,66` | wet patches: flat ovals `98,112,108`, five texels wide with a light texel |

Three kinds are built things and keep straight edges, tile for tile, instead of the rounded masks:

| Kind | Look |
|---|---|
| Paving (the ground of cities, and the ancient floor of Spear Pillar) | stone slabs 16 texels square, `204,206,214`, one in four a shade darker `194,198,208`, with a joint line `168,172,188` along each slab's east and south edge. Where paving meets other ground it ends in a kerb two texels wide, `150,154,172` with a light line `226,228,234` inside it |
| Planks (bridge decks, boardwalks) | boards 8 texels wide laid across the way one walks, `178,134,92`, every third one a shade lighter `192,150,104`; a groove `126,90,62` between boards, a nail texel `96,70,52` at each end, and a dark rail line `104,74,54` along the deck's open sides |
| Stairs | treads 8 texels deep in stone `184,178,170`: a light nose `214,210,204` on the edge that faces downhill and the riser's shade `122,116,124` under it, so a flight reads as steps whichever way it climbs |

### Relief

Height comes from the map (plan 01 · M3) and is drawn true to scale: one tile up is one world unit, which the camera shows about half a tile tall.

- **The lowlands stay flat.** A map's ground level is drawn at zero, exactly as a map without heights is.
- **Small steps are slopes.** Where neighbouring tiles differ by less than three quarters of a tile, the lower one rises to meet the higher across its own width (`Relief`): a kerb or a beach is a gentle bank, never a sliver of wall.
- **Water lies level with its banks.** Sinnoh's water is half a tile under the ground around it; the game says that with the painted bank (see Water) and draws the surface level with the shore (at the whole tile above the water's own height), so a pond is a flat sheet of pixel art and a river runs level under its bridges.
- **A waterfall** is a sheet of falling water down the slope between two levels: streaks of light `150,206,246` and of foam `236,246,255` on mid-blue `76,146,214`, each with a dark line `46,104,186` beside it, and clumps of foam where the water goes over the edge and where it lands. It doesn't move yet.
- **A step of three quarters of a tile or more is a face**: a wall from the lower ground up to the higher, drawn where it faces south, east or west (a face that looks north is never seen). Its art keeps square texels on screen, so it has 32 texel columns and about 16 rows to the tile:
  - under lawn, flowers, tall grass, paths and sand: an **earth bank**, the ledge's face carried down: bright edge `160,222,122`, scalloped grass lip `120,200,102`, then dirt `178,138,90` with a broken strata line `146,108,72` every six rows
  - under rock, snow, ice and cave floors: a **rock face** in the boulder's shades: a light top edge `196,192,190`, then beds of `150,146,150` of uneven thickness, each lit along its top `176,170,166` with a longer glint or two, shaded `104,100,112` where it overhangs the next, and cracked `74,70,86` in one or two places part of the way down. Long beds and few cracks: strata, never a wall of blocks
- **Everything stands on the ground it is on**: trees, grass, props, buildings and people are lifted with their tile, and the camera follows the player's height.
- **Whoever walks under a bridge goes under it.** A deck stands lower over the ground than a person's card is tall once the steep camera's stretch is on it (Route 205's footbridge stands two tiles over the grass), so a person under one is cut off at the deck's height over the deck's own tiles (`Relief.DeckOver`, the sprite program's `deckOver`): the boards hide them as the original's bridge layer does, and no head pokes up through the planks. Whoever stands on the deck, or beside it, is drawn whole.
- **Faces cast shadows; flat ground does not**, as before. Ambient occlusion darkens the foot of a face.
- **Ledges** face south, west or east. The side ones are the same ridge turned: the grass lip runs along the top of the drop and the dirt face shows as a narrow strip, since the camera sees it edge on.
- **A bridge over the sea** (plan 22 · U6, part b: the Driftveil Drawbridge, whose import is its deck and nothing round it) is a deck of planks at the bridge's own height with the sea five tiles under it on every side and under its boards (`WorldMapEntry.SeaBelow`, `WorldMapBuilder.DeckOverTheSea`): the boards are the deck's planks and its open sides show the deck's beam, the water runs on under it as under any deck, and its railings are planks the way doesn't open. The four columns either side of a way in stay ground at the deck's height, its abutments, and stand on an earth face down to the sea. It is seen with a camera of its own (`FieldCamera.Drawbridge`: 760 units off, pitched 48°, 17° of view), lower and further than the overworld's, so the sea beside the deck shows how high it stands.

### Caves

A cave (plan 01 · M5) is the field with rock where the open country has forest: the same pixel grid, the same sprites, the same relief, and no sky.

- **Rock is whatever the cave doesn't open.** Every tile that blocks the way without being water or something built is the cave's rock, and so is the space beyond the walls that nobody can reach. Its top is dark rock: base `66,58,70`, lighter patches `78,70,80`, a rim of `44,38,52` where it meets the floor, cracks `52,46,60`.
- **Walls stand up from the floor, by the rules of relief.** Rock behind a floor (to its north) or beside it stands two tiles above it and shows the rock face of "Relief" where it looks south, east or west. Rock right in front of a floor (the row south of it) is a lip of three quarters of a tile: the least that is still a step with a face (anything lower would be joined to the floor by a slope, and the floor would rise to meet the rock), and low enough to hide no more than the feet of whoever walks behind it. The rock behind the lip stands the full two tiles, which hides the lip and ends at the floor's edge. A wall one tile thick between two passages is a lip too.
- **Whatever can't be come to is rock**: the floor is what can be reached from the cave's ways in. The space past the walls, and any pocket of floor the walls close in, is rock like the rest.
- **The floor** is the cave-floor ground of "Ground" (`112,100,104`); rocks standing on it stand on it, not on a square of earth.
- **The camera is the original's for caves**: nearer and steeper than the overworld's (63.26° against 59.05°, with a wider lens), so walls hide less. The view stops at the cave's own edge and never shows what lies past its rock.
- **A cave of one chunk is as large as its floor and a band of rock round it** (`WorldMapBuilder.CaveOfOneChunk`): the original lays a small cave (the Solaceon Ruins' rooms, a dead end, a floor of a temple) in the corner of its chunk and leaves the rest dark. Past the floor the map keeps as much rock east and south as it has west and north (at least two tiles, never more than the chunk), and beyond that is the cave's dark `24,20,26`. So a small room sits in the middle of the picture, with its walls round it, and the rock's lighter patches never spread out into a field that reads as floor.
- **The light ignores the clock** (`ArtLook.CaveFieldRig`): a warm key from nearly overhead and a little to the south-west `0.60,0.52,0.42` (so a wall's shadow stays on the lip before it and never lies across a passage), cool blue shade from above `0.44,0.47,0.58` and a dim bounce from the floor `0.36,0.33,0.32`, so the floor reads at about three quarters of its daylight value and the walls' faces a little brighter than their tops. The space past the rock is `24,20,26`, the colour the walls' tops fall to. A strong vignette (0.40) closes the picture in; no fog, no cloud shade, no lamps.
- **No weather comes in**, except fog, which some caves are full of.
- **A dark cave** (the original's one: Wayward Cave) shows a circle of the floor round the player and nothing else (`Darkness`): the picture falls to `8,6,12` outside a radius of 2.2 tiles, soft over the next 1.2. The circle lies on the ground, so on screen it is wider than it is tall, and it is the one soft edge in the field besides mist. Once Flash has been used there (from the party menu, plan 02 · S2) the cave is lit like any other, until the player leaves the caves.
- **The Battle Pyramid's dark** (plan 14 · B10; `BattlePyramidChallenge`, `PyramidFloor`) is the same dark over a floor of the cave's ground with rocks for walls, inside a hall's walls (`interior: Gym`, 34 tiles by 36: the floor 32 square, the back wall three deep), but its own: Flash lifts none of it and isn't offered there (`Map.FlashLifts`). Each floor begins with a circle of Emerald's 32 pixels, two tiles, soft over the next 1.2 as a cave's; every battle won on the floor (a wild Pokémon's too, as Emerald's script has it) widens it by four pixels, a quarter of a tile, to at most 120 (seven and a half tiles), the edge moving out at three tiles a second as Dewford's does. The stairs up are a flight of the floor's own (`s`) in the middle of a square of the floor's grid; the way in is the middle of another square. Its trainers stand in the dark like anyone, and are seen only once the circle reaches them.
- **Machines in a mine** are the mine's own (see "The mine", under Props); what the world blocks under their models carries them.
- **Don't**: light a cave with the time of day, grow lawn or trees in one, or stand a wall taller than the lip in front of a floor.

#### Hideaways

The twelve hideaways under the Underground (plan 38 · EX6; `Models/Underground/Hideaways.cs`) are caves laid by rule, the Battle Pyramid's way (`FloorLayout`), but lit: no dark, no circle, the cave's own light rig and camera (a map file whose `setting` is `Cave`). Each is three squares by three of eight tiles inside a band of the cave's rock two tiles deep and a back wall three deep, 28 tiles by 29, so the whole hideaway is about a picture and a half and the camera follows the player down it as it does a long room.

- **A hideaway's ground is the field's own grounds, chosen by its habitat**, never a new texture: the floor is one ground (cave floor `c`, sand `,`, snow `^` or earth `;`), and patches of a second (tall grass `w`, flowers `*`, marsh `m` or sand) lie on it where its seed's noise is high, never on the two tiles at the middle of a square's side, so the joints between squares are always walked. Pools are the field's water (`~`) in a hideaway of the water; in any other they are the cave's rock.
- **The pieces are rock and water only**: a piece says where rock stands (`R`) and where a pool lies (`~`); the ground is laid over the rest by the rule above. Every piece is open at the middle two tiles of each side, as the Pyramid's are, and its floor is one piece without its pools.
- **The twelve and their grounds**: the fern grotto (cave floor, tall grass, pools), the lake cavern (cave floor, flowers, wide pools), the crystal hall (cave floor, sand), the ice hall (snow, sand), the ember hall (earth, sand), the sand hall (sand, earth), the shade hollow (cave floor, tall grass), the spark vein (earth, cave floor), the sky shaft (cave floor, tall grass, flowers), the mire pool (cave floor, marsh, pools), the dragon's deep (cave floor, sand, pools) and the echo den (earth, tall grass). Ice that slides and lava that glows are not laid yet: a hideaway is walked end to end on foot, and neither has a ground of a map file.
- **The way out** is a cave's mouth in the back wall over the middle of the north row's middle square, and whoever comes down stands on the floor before it facing south.
- **Its Pokémon stand about in it**: a hideaway shows its wild Pokémon whatever the options' WILD POKéMON row says (`Map.WildVisible`), on any floor where they can be met, not only in its grass, as "Wild Pokémon in sight" draws them.
- **Don't**: paint a ground of its own for one hideaway, lay ice or water on a square's joints, or darken a hideaway.

#### Caves that are built

A place the original builds as a cave that is really a building (plan 20, wave 77; an overlay's `caveLook`, `Map.CaveLook`) keeps everything above (the camera, the light, the lip, the rock standing two tiles up) but is built in its own material, tile for tile with straight edges as decks and paving are (`BuiltArt.PaintGround`), where a cave's ground is rounded:

- **Nothing beyond its walls.** Rock with no floor within two tiles of it is no part of the building: it is void (`World.DarkBeyondTheWalls`), the dark round the place, never a slab of rock top the size of the chunk (the Ruins of Alph's hall, whose chunk runs on south of its walls).
- **The Ruins of Alph's stone** (`Ruins`: the four chambers, the hall's entrance and the underground hall). The floor is flagstones of worn sandstone 32 by 16 texels, each course half a stone along from the last, in three tones `166,140,104` / `176,150,112` / `186,160,120`, a joint `120,100,76` along the east and south, a light edge a quarter lighter along the west and north, and a crack in about one stone in sixteen. What the original lays in pale tiles is dressed blocks of paler stone the same size (`196,176,136` / `206,186,146`, joint `140,118,88`), one in five carved with a line three texels in from its edge. The top of its rock is rough-dressed blocks 20 by 12 (`94,80,64` / `104,90,72`, joints `66,56,46`). The faces between levels are courses of sandstone twelve rows high (`170,146,110`, lit `196,172,132`, shaded `140,118,88`, joints `100,84,64`), blocks 24 wide, each course half a block along, under a pale coping `214,194,154` with a lit top `232,214,176`. **What is read is a stele, not a signboard**: an upright slab of sandstone (`186,160,120`, lit west, shaded east) 20 texels wide on a plinth, its head's corners rounded off, cut with three rows of three small glyphs of our own (rings, bars and hooks, never any game's letters) in `116,96,70`.
- **The Burned Tower's timbers** (`Charred`). The floor is charred floorboards 8 deep running east and west, joints staggered (`56,42,36` / `64,48,40` / `74,56,46`, grooves `28,22,22`), with ash lying grey along a board here and there (`104,98,94`, its top row `126,120,114`) and a rare ember `170,74,40` two texels across. What the original lays in pale tiles is the boards that survived, scorched brown (`110,82,60` / `122,92,66` / `132,100,72`, grooves `70,52,40`) and sooted darker toward each end. The top of its rock is a heap of burnt beams (`42,34,32` / `50,40,36`, lit along their tops `70,56,48`, gaps `24,20,20`). The faces between levels are charred upright planks eight texels wide (`60,46,40`, burnt black `40,32,30` and ash-grey `96,90,86` in patches) under a burnt beam. Nothing of it glows: the embers are paint.
- **Don't**: round a built cave's floor into the rock as a cave's is, or show its rock past the walls.

### Rock outdoors

The world's own heights are for whoever walks: a mountainside nobody can set foot on has none, and by them would lie as flat as the road beside it. So rock that blocks the way follows the rule of a cave's rock (plan 01 · M5).

- **Rock nobody walks on stands up**: two tiles above the nearest ground that isn't such rock, or a lip of three quarters of a tile where open ground lies right behind it (to its north). It shows the rock face of "Relief" to the south, east and west, and its top is the rock ground of "Ground". Rock that is walked on (a mountain path, Oreburgh's approach) stays at its own height.
- **The mouth of a cave** is a dark hollow at the level of the ground, in the rock that stands round it: `24,20,30` with patches of `30,26,38`, a rim of `12,10,18`. It lies where the original paints its own dark (the importer knows the texture by name): behind the tile that is the way in (Oreburgh Gate, entered from the side), or round it, in which case the way in is as dark as the rest and still walked on (the Ravaged Path, entered northward: a slot in the foot of a rock face). Inside a cave the same dark lies on the one tile of rock just past each exit mat (`Exit…`: the way the mat is walked off), at the mat's height, so a way out is a gap in the wall and never reads as more rock.
- **The way into a forest** (plan 01 · M6: Eterna Forest, Floaroma Meadow) is the dark under the trees at the edge of the treeline, at the level of the ground: `34,40,30` with patches of `42,50,36`, a rim of `20,24,18`. It lies where the original paints its own (`fenter`); as with a cave's mouth, the dark is walked on where it is the way in and blocks where it is the shade beyond, and no tree grows in it.
- **A Bicycle's ramp** (Wayward Cave, Oreburgh Gate) is a short run of steps up to the lip it is jumped from: blocked on foot, jumped on a Bicycle going its way (three tiles on in top gear, one in low).
- **Don't** raise rock one walks on, grow a tree in a cave's mouth, or leave a mountain as a grey floor.

### The Distortion World

The Distortion World (plan 01 · M8) is islands of stone floating over nothing: the field's pixel grid, sprites and relief, with no ground under the islands and no sky over them. Each floor is a map of its own (the setting `Void`).

- **The void is nothing**: every tile that blocks the way without being rock, water, grass or a tree is nothing, and so is a gap the original jumps across, and everything past the map's edge. Nothing has no ground and no face of its own: the picture shows the background there.
- **An island's edge over the void is its underside**: rock hanging three tiles down (`WorldMapBuilder.VoidDrop`) and ending in points of uneven length. The art is 128 texels wide and 48 rows tall, stretched once down the drop: a pale edge `164,154,180`, two beds of rock `112,102,130`, each lit along its top `138,128,156` and shaded under `86,78,104`, then the hanging rock in two darker steps `92,84,112` and `70,62,90`, and the points outlined in `52,46,70`. Below the points the art is empty, so the void shows through. The underside hangs from the island's edge, however deep the void lies beside it (an upper stone floats over a lower floor).
- **The islands' stone** is a ground of its own: base `122,112,138`, lighter patches `136,126,152`, a rim `84,74,100` and cracks `100,90,118`. Ledges between islands are ridges of the same stone, with a paler line `170,160,190` along the top of the drop.
- **Rock rims an island**: the original's rock stands three quarters of a tile above the stone beside it (the least that is still a step with a face), so the paths between rims stay in view. Its faces are the rock face of "Relief".
- **A way somewhere is a slab set into the stone** (a tile of its own, `DistortionSlab`): a joint `76,68,94` round a slab `170,162,188`, lit `204,198,218` on its top and left and shaded `118,108,138` on its bottom and right, with a four-pointed star `226,218,244` in the middle. Only the ways have one; the original's other marks on its floors are the stone like the rest.
- **The sea under B3F**: water that reaches the map's edge is a sea far under the islands, one level under the lowest of them, filling the nothing round them; its water is the field's.
- **The light ignores the clock** (`ArtLook.VoidFieldRig`): a dim, cool key from high over the left `0.50,0.48,0.60`, violet light from all round `0.46,0.42,0.58` and a cold teal bounce from below `0.30,0.36,0.38`. The background is a deep violet `36,30,50`, and far islands fade into `0.16,0.13,0.21` (40 % at most, from 40 units to 76). The grade drains the colour (saturation 0.78, contrast 1.08), shade leans violet `0.92,0.86,1.16`, highlights a little green `0.98,1.03,1.0`, bloom only above 0.92 at 0.2, and a strong vignette (0.42) closes the picture in. No sky, no cloud shade, no lamps, no weather.
- **The camera is the field's**, and it shows past the islands' edges into the void (no overscan of forest or rock).
- **What the story puts there** (plan 02 · S12) is the field's own: a **portal** is the tear in the world the people's kit already has (the look `Rift`: a dark core with a red heart, ringed by two turning bands of light), standing where the original hangs its portal; the three guardians' **boulders** are the field's round boulder, and a **pit** is a tile of nothing let into an island beside the path a boulder is pushed along (the tile the original drops its boulder through), so the rim is broken there and the hole behind it comes down to the path. A boulder pushed over a pit keeps level with the floor it left as far as the lip and sinks from there, six tenths of a tile into the pit's mouth by the time it is in; one whose pit it is rests there, its top above the floor, and is no longer pushed, and any other is gone. Nothing else is drawn for a pit. Giratina and the guardians stand as their field sprites, over the void where the original floats them, at the height of the island beside them (Giratina most of a tile higher, clear of whoever stands before it); one the original floats over an island's rim stands on the rim.
- **Don't** draw ground under the void, light it by the hour, give a void tile a face, or grow forest past a floor's edge.

### Climates

A few places have air of their own, which changes their light beyond the hour and the weather (plan 01 · M10, part b; an area's overlay names its `climate`, `ArtLook.InClimate` lays it over the rig the place would have had, and the light eases over a second at an area's border as snow country's does). The ground, the rock and the people are drawn as everywhere: the climate is light and grade alone, and what burns.

- **Stark Mountain in the open** (`Volcanic`): ash hangs in the air, under the slow ashfall its header gives it. The sun is dimmer and redder (0.82 of itself, tinted `1.06,0.93,0.82`), the sky's light a little warm `1.04,0.95,0.90`, the bounce off the rock warmer `1.2,1.0,0.86`. An ashen haze `0.50,0.44,0.42` takes seven tenths of the fog's colour, thickens it by 0.14 and brings it in (from 40 units to 66 at most). The shade turns from the field's violet to umber `1.02,0.90,0.96`, the highlights warm `1.08,0.99,0.88`, the colour is a little drained (0.92). No cloud shade drifts over it.
- **Inside Stark Mountain** (`Volcanic`, a cave): the rock is lit from below by its own heat. The cave's key keeps its place and turns a dull amber `0.66,0.47,0.34`, the light from above a red-violet `0.47,0.36,0.40`, and the bounce off the floor a red-orange `0.54,0.30,0.20`, stronger than the cave's. The picture ends in `30,16,18` instead of the cave's grey, shade leans red `1.08,0.86,0.90`, highlights amber `1.10,0.98,0.84`. The same rock, the same faces: only the heat is new. **People keep their own colours there** (plan 01 · M12): people and Pokémon standing in that light are lit by a grey as bright as it (`ArtLook.ForPeople`, `LightRig.PeopleWash` 1: each of the key, the light from above and the bounce turned to its own brightness), and take their warmth from the grade over the picture alone. Lit by the amber itself, a pale uniform or a white coat came out the colour of a face and read as bare skin. The rock keeps all of it, and so do the things of the map that stand among it (a boulder, a cracked rock, an item's ball).
- **What burns glows by day**: in a volcano's light whatever the art marks as lit (lava, the crater's throat, the seams in the mountain's faces) glows at 0.75 at every hour, as lamps do after dark elsewhere, and no brighter after dark. Its glow is lava's own orange `1.0,0.58,0.20`, not lamplight: lit by lamplight at a lamp's full strength the cracks turned pale yellow at night and the crater's throat into a moon. So lava is bright without being pale: its yellow stays under the bloom's threshold and the glow does the rest.
- **Snowpoint Temple** (`Frozen`, a cave): stone and ice under a cold light. A pale blue-white key `0.56,0.60,0.68`, blue from above `0.42,0.50,0.66`, a cyan bounce off the ice `0.34,0.42,0.50`; the picture ends in `16,22,36`; shade leans blue `0.82,0.90,1.20`, highlights cold `0.96,1.0,1.08`, the colour is drained by a tenth, and bloom keeps to what is brighter than the ice (threshold 0.95, half strength), as in snow country. Its ice is the field's own ice, its pillars the temples' columns, its statue a giant of cold stone ("Props").
- **Verdant** (plan 23: Resolution Cave, Poni, where Zygarde waits at the bottom): inside only, rock lit by the green cells drifting out of its cracks. The cave's key from overhead brighter and a little green `0.70,0.70,0.60`, light from all round a green grey `0.48,0.56,0.54`, a green bounce off the floor `0.42,0.48,0.40`, the background `16,28,22`, the fog `0.06,0.11,0.08`, shade leaning green `0.88,1.02,0.98`, highlights `1.02,1.04,0.94`. Outdoors it changes nothing. So that a dark Pokémon at the bottom of the cave stands out from its floor, never as a lamp: nothing is marked as lit.
- **Don't** repaint a place's rock for its climate, let lava bloom to white, or grade a room (a climate is for the open and for caves).

### Seasons

Unova alone has seasons (plan 22 · U3; `Region.HasSeasons`): a month each of the real calendar, round and round as Black 2 counts them (January spring, February summer, March autumn, April winter, May spring again), unless the options fix one. Everywhere else nothing of this exists, and no map of another region bakes, lights or tells anything otherwise. A season is the lawn, the grass, the trees, the flowers and the light: the paving, the buildings, the water, the rock, the ledges and the people are drawn as in every season. The palettes are `Graphics/Seasons.cs` (`SeasonPalette.For`), which `StyleGuideTests` holds to these numbers.

| | Lawn (base, patches) | Tufts (four shades, darkest first) | Tall grass (four shades) | Forest floor, tall grass's ground | Round crowns (tint between) | Pines (tint between) | Flowers (three kinds) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Spring** | the field's own: `104,190,98`, `120,200,102` | the field's own (`70,150,82` to `160,222,122`) | the field's own (`36,110,62` to `170,232,130`) | `58,126,82`, `48,124,70` | `96,176,104` to `118,192,108` | `104,178,118` to `126,196,128` | white, red, yellow, as everywhere |
| **Summer** | `86,176,84`, `102,188,90` | `56,134,70`, `72,156,76`, `102,188,90`, `146,212,108` | `28,98,54`, `50,138,68`, `92,184,86`, `156,224,116` | `50,116,74`, `40,114,62` | `80,164,90` to `100,180,94` | `92,168,106` to `112,186,116` | yellow `252,214,72`, orange `244,140,52`, white |
| **Autumn** | `176,170,92`, `192,182,104` | `128,112,58`, `156,138,70`, `192,182,104`, `222,206,130` | `112,92,46`, `150,122,58`, `196,166,82`, `232,206,120` | `120,92,58`, `116,96,52` | `214,120,58` to `226,164,70` | `96,150,100` to `112,164,108` | orange `236,120,60`, deep red `196,70,64`, ochre `232,190,96` |
| **Winter** | snow: `186,204,232`, `196,212,238` | frosted stalks `132,138,150`, `156,164,178`, `196,206,224`, `226,232,244` | dry grass under snow `92,104,96`, `128,140,124`, `176,190,196`, `226,232,244` | `150,168,200`, `120,140,150` | snow on the crowns: `208,218,236` to `222,230,242` | the snowbound pine: white tips over its green | snowdrops: white, pale blue `196,214,240`, white |

- **Spring is the field as it has always been**: a chunk of Unova baked in spring is the chunk baked with no season at all, texel for texel.
- **Winter's snow is a look**: it lies on every tile of lawn and on every tree, and the ground under it walks as the decompilation says (no prints, no sinking) unless a winter layout gives the tile a snow behaviour (plan 22 · U4). It is the snow of the ground's own `Snow` kind, so it stays as far below white as snow does everywhere.
- **The light** (`ArtLook.Seasoned`, laid over the rig after the weather's): spring is the hour's own. Summer: the sun 1.04 of itself tinted `1.03,1.0,0.94`, colour 1.06. Autumn: the sun tinted `1.08,0.98,0.86`, the sky's light `1.04,0.98,0.92`, colour 1.04, highlights `1.06,1.0,0.90`. Winter: the sun 0.86 of itself tinted `0.94,0.98,1.06`, the sky's light `0.96,0.99,1.05`, the bounce off the ground 1.1, three tenths of the fog's colour toward `0.82,0.88,0.96`, colour 0.92, bloom only above 0.95 and at 0.6 of its strength, shade `0.90,0.94,1.12`. Rooms and caves take no season's light.
- **A season's turn** is seen at the next fade: the scenes are made again behind it, and the field's toast tells it ("Spring has come to Unova."). A game loaded or begun takes the season of its day without a word.
- **Don't** give a season to anything but the lawn, the grass, the trees, the flowers and the light; let winter's lawn go whiter than the ground's snow; or let a season reach a map of another region.

### Water

Water lies in the ground plane and is drawn texel by texel (`PixelGround` bakes a shore-distance mask, the water shader colours it), so shores are rounded pixel art and nothing is a smooth gradient.

| Element | Value |
|---|---|
| Depth bands | shallow `116,184,232` to 7 texels from the shore, mid `76,146,214` to 20, deep `58,118,190` beyond; the band edges breathe by a texel |
| Foam | `236,246,255`: a line at the shore 1–3 texels wide that laps in and out, and a broken second line 3–7 texels out |
| Wave marks | light dashes `150,206,246` 4–8 texels long with a dark line `46,104,186` under them and their ends dipped, drifting east two texels a second, never nearer than 9 texels to the shore |
| Sparkles | white plus-signs that flash for a moment, in sunlight only |
| North bank | where land lies to the north the bank shows as a face 5 texels deep, `124,116,116` over `88,84,98`; other shores get a one-texel light rim |
| Motion | everything moves in whole texels; no sub-texel scrolling |
| Strong current | a tile whose water carries a surfer (`TileBehavior.CurrentEast`, `West`, `North`, `South`: the GBA games' currents, Alola's reef gap) shows its flow. Streaks one texel thick lie along the flow, one to a cell 16 texels along by 4 across (about four cells in five hold one), each 6 to 11 texels long: a head of foam `236,246,255` two texels long at its leading end, a body of thin foam `176,218,248`, a last texel of wave light `150,206,246`, and the wave marks' dark line `46,104,186` under all but that last texel. They run 8 texels a second along the flow, stepping a whole texel at a time (`floor` of the time, never scrolled), over the bands, the wave marks and the broken foam, but never over the shore's own lapping line. Where a streak lies nothing is mirrored. The water's mask carries the flow in its green: 64 east, 128 west, 192 north, 255 south, nought for still water |
| Reflection | what stands by the water is mirrored in it (plan 09 · L10): the people, Pokémon and things of the map, item balls, buildings, props and trees, never the ground, grass, lamplight or what the field's life draws. In the mid and deep bands only, never on foam, a wave mark or a sparkle, which lie over it. One colour per field texel (read at the texel's middle), each row of texels pushed east or west by a whole texel on a slow swell (`round(1.2 · sin(1.5 t + 0.9 row))`), and broken: runs of six texels drift east with the wave marks and about one in six is left plain. The reflection is darkened to the band it lies in: `mix(reflection, band, 0.35) × 0.82`, laid over the band at 70 %. Only the water of one height mirrors (the height most of the water in view lies at; ties go to the water nearest the middle of the picture): water above or below it shows its bands alone. There is no sky to mirror, so where nothing stands over the water it is the water's own colour. After dark lit windows mirror as lit. On the Low preset nothing is mirrored |

**Puddles** (plan 01 · M7; `TileType.Puddle`, from the behaviours `Puddle` and `StillPuddle`) are a ground of their own, not water: they are walked through and lie flat in the ground plane like sand or ice, painted by the same rule (a base, lighter patches, a rim where they meet other ground, marks), so neighbouring puddle tiles run together into one pool with a rounded edge.

| Element | Value |
|---|---|
| Base | `112,142,170`: the sky, dimmed by the mud under it |
| Patches | `138,168,194`, where it catches the sky |
| Rim | `92,80,68`, dark wet earth, where the puddle meets other ground |
| Marks | a glint `196,218,232`, a short stroke or a ring two texels across |
| Reflection | whoever stands in one is mirrored in it: their sprite upside down on the ground under their feet, as long as it looks tall (its height over the sine of the camera's pitch), tinted `150,170,196`, fading from 45 % at the feet to nothing, and cut off where the puddle ends to the south. A puddle takes nothing from the water's reflection: the walker is all the original's puddles mirror |
| Polished floor | a tile of the behaviour `ShinyFloor` (the Hall of Fame and the hallway to it, the ruins' floors) mirrors whoever stands on it as a puddle does, with a tint of its own, `176,182,222`, fading from 35 % at the feet: pale on the dark polished stone, so the walker shows without the floor turning grey. A run that crosses from a puddle to a floor takes the tint of the tile under the feet |

**Whirlpools** (plan 20 · J8; `PropType.Whirlpool`, placed by HeartGold's model `uzushio` over a block of three tiles by three of the whirlpool behaviour) are a sheet lying two texels over the water, its own pixel art in four frames, six a second: the water itself still shows round it.

| Element | Value |
|---|---|
| Size | a disc 92 texels across, centred on the block of three tiles; nothing outside the disc is drawn |
| Rings | three arms of a spiral from the rim to the eye, light `150,206,246` on the deep band `58,118,190`, each arm 3 texels wide, with a dark line `36,84,160` on its inner side |
| Eye | a dark hole `28,62,128`, 14 texels across, with a one-texel rim of foam `236,246,255` |
| Foam | broken dashes of foam on the outer rim, 4–8 texels long |
| Motion | the arms turn a twelfth of a turn a frame, in whole texels; the rim's foam stays where it is |

#### The sea floor

The sea floor (plan 21 · H5; a map whose setting is `Underwater`, `HoennUnderwater`) lies tile for tile under the sea it is dived to from. It is the field's own pixel ground, sprites and relief under a light of its own; the surface is never seen from the steep camera, so nothing is drawn for it.

- **Ground**: the field's sand where the floor is swum, the field's rock where it is blocked (stood up as rock is everywhere, `RaiseRock`), and sand under everything, so no lawn shows where rock's rounded corners meet the floor. Nothing grows there but kelp: no forest past the floor's edge, no trees, no houses.
- **The kelp bed** (`TileType.Kelp`, the GBA's seaweed) is a ground of its own under the fronds: base `150,156,112`, patches `166,170,124`, a rim `108,120,84` where it meets the sand, and holdfasts `92,112,76` (a knot two texels wide with a root splayed to each side).
- **Kelp** (`NatureArt.Kelp`, 32 × 32, repeating along a row) stands a tile tall in one row across the middle of each kelp tile, cut out by the field shader, swaying and parting round a diver as tall grass does. Four fronds to the texture, each a stalk two texels thick, `44,82,64` at its holdfast, `62,110,76` up to half way, `92,140,88` above and a tip of `132,170,104`, its side texel a shade darker; it waves a texel either way every four rows, two in its top third, and puts out a blade of leaf a shade lighter every five rows, to the left and the right in turn.
- **The light ignores the clock** (`ArtLook.UnderwaterFieldRig`), as a cave's does: a blue-green key from nearly overhead `0.50,0.66,0.66`, blue light from all round `0.38,0.50,0.62`, a little warmth bounced off the sand `0.36,0.40,0.36`. The background is a deep sea-blue `22,58,78`; a blue fog `0.10,0.30,0.38` takes half the colour from 36 units to 60. The colour is drained (saturation 0.80, contrast 1.04), shade leans blue `0.84,0.96,1.12`, highlights a little green `0.94,1.04,1.02`, bloom only above 0.90 at 0.22, vignette 0.34. No sky, no cloud shade, no lamps, no weather.
- **Caustics** (plan 21 · H5, part b; `WeatherFx.Caustics`, `LifeArt.Caustics`): the light the waves above throw down, a net of bright lines laid over the whole picture after the composite, as the mist is, but in whole texels. The net is one texture, 64 texels square and repeating, point-filtered: the edges between the cells of a Worley pattern of four cells by four (sixteen texels apart, each cell's point at its own place), a core where the second-nearest point is less than 0.8 texel further than the nearest (alpha 255) and a fringe out to 1.6 (alpha 110); nothing elsewhere. Two layers of it are added over the picture, one at 6 layout units to the texel (two of the field's texels at 1080p) drifting 9 units a second east and 4 down, one at 9 (three field texels) drifting 12 units a second west and 6 down, in `196,236,228` at alpha 34 and 26, dimmed by the scene's light as the mist is. Each offset is held to the 3-unit grid, so every edge of the net falls on a texel of the field, and pinned to the sea floor: it moves with the ground as the camera follows the diver (the camera's target times a tile's size on the picture), so the light lies on the floor and drifts over it, never slides with the screen. Timed by `FieldLife.Now`. The camera stays the field's.
- **Bubbles** (plan 21 · H5, part b; `FieldLife.Bubbles`, cells 13 to 15 of the life atlas): the diver breathes out every 2.8 seconds of `FieldLife.Now` (at whole multiples of it, so the same breaths however the frames fall; after a pause of more than half a second none is made up), three bubbles a tenth of a second apart from over the head (1.25 above the ground), each a few texels to one side by its breath and its number. A bubble rises 24 texels a second in whole texels and sways a texel aside and back every fifth of a second. For its first third of a second it is small (cell 13: a ring 4 texels across round a glint), then grown (cell 14: a ring 6 texels across with a body at alpha 90 inside, so it reads over pale sand, and a glint of two texels at its upper left), and for its last 0.15 s of 1.3 it bursts (cell 15: four specks a texel each flying apart). Tinted `214,236,250`; water, so it dims half as far as the ground does. A breath is heard (`bubbles`, *docs/sound-effects.md*).
- **In battle** the sea floor is a stage of its own (`BattleArena.Underwater`, "Arenas (G8)"), whatever the tile.
- **Don't** draw the sea's surface overhead, grade the sea floor by the hour, or let the sand bloom to white. Don't blur the caustics as the mist is blurred, or let them slide over the floor with the camera; don't let a bubble rise smoothly between texels.

### Grass, flowers, trees, ledges and rocks

- **Tall grass**: two rows of clumps per tile, each clump five blades in four flat shades (`36,110,62`, `62,150,78`, `106,196,98`, tips `170,232,130`), 16 texels tall, the rows half a clump out of step. They sway at the top and lean away from anyone walking through.
- **Lawn tufts and flowers**: single upright cards facing the camera (crossed cards read as scribbles from the steep camera). Tufts are a small clump in the lawn's shades; flowers are a stem, two leaves and a five-texel blossom, mostly white, some red or yellow.
- **Trees in snow country** (an area with more snow than lawn: Snowpoint City, Routes 216 and 217, not Twinleaf Town with its few patches): every tier of a pine is white at its tip and keeps its green toward its rim.
- **Trees**: grey textures in four flat tones (118, 162, 206, 244) tinted per tree. Pine tiers are bands of scalloped needles with a toothed rim; round crowns are overlapping leaf clumps, each with a light crescent on the upper left and a dark one on the lower right. Bark is `116,80,54` with dark grooves `86,58,42` and a light line beside each. No per-texel random variation.
- **Ledges**: a ridge 12 texels high: a bright edge `160,222,122`, a grass lip (`120,200,102`, scalloped) over a dirt face `178,138,90` with one broken strata line `146,108,72` and a dark base `116,84,60`.
- **Rocks**: a boulder is a sprite like the other props: a big stone with a smaller one at its foot, in three flat shades (`196,192,190`, `150,146,150`, `104,100,112`) with a crack line `74,70,86` and an outline; every other one is mirrored. In water it stands a little lower, in a ring of foam.
- **Obstacles** (what Cut, Rock Smash and Strength clear) are sprites that can be told from the scenery and from each other at a glance: a **small tree** with a thin trunk and a round crown in the tall grass's greens; a **cracked rock** in browns (`204,168,126`, `168,128,92`, `122,90,70`) split right across by cracks `78,56,50`; a **round boulder**, smooth and grey (`214,210,204`, `164,160,162`, `112,108,122`) with one glint and one dimple. They are objects of the map like an item's ball (plan 02 · S2), cards standing a little south of their tile's middle (20 texels in, 19 for the rock) with a contact patch under them a little smaller than a person's, so one that gives way or is pushed changes nothing of the scenery round it.
- **Rubble** (plan 02 · S12) is what is left of something the story broke, where the original stands such a heap as an object of the map (the shards of Mt. Coronet's cave painting, either side of the hole Team Galactic broke through it): a card like an obstacle's, 30 by 20, standing 19 texels into its tile, of three broken slabs leaning on one another in the cave rock's own tones lifted to be read against the floor (`150,138,150`, `118,106,122`, `84,74,92`, edges `58,50,66`), the largest showing two strokes of the painting's ochre `196,108,64`. No field move clears it and nothing pushes it: it stands in the way, and is read.
- **The cave painting** (plan 02 · S12) is the slab the rubble was: a card 32 wide and 52 tall, a whole tile across, standing 26 texels into its tile so that it fills the niche it is set in and covers the dark of the way behind it. One upright stone with a rounded head in the rubble's own tones (`150,138,150`, `118,106,122`, `84,74,92`, edges `58,50,66`), a course of darker stone at its foot, and on it the painting in two colours only, the ochre `196,108,64` and a chalk `222,210,196`: a ring with three small marks set round it in a triangle, and under them two beasts drawn as a few strokes each, face to face. The drawing is our own, of what the game's own words say is painted there. It is in the way like the rubble, is read, and is gone when the story breaks it.
- **A carved wall of the Ruins of Alph** (plan 20 · J5's last leftover) is the cave painting's slab cut in the Ruins' own sandstone: the same card, 32 by 52, standing 26 texels into its tile over the way through a chamber's back wall, its head squared off, in the stone's tones (`176,150,112`, `146,122,90`, `108,88,66`, edges `72,58,44`), a darker course at its foot, and on it four rows of small carved signs, each a few strokes cut in shadow (`88,70,52`) with their lower lip caught in light (`198,176,138`): rings with a tick, bars, crosses and hooks, a script that is no letters of ours. It stands in the way, is read, and is gone when its secret is brought.
- **The Magma Stone** (plan 02 · S15, part b) is the stone at the head of Stark Mountain's last room, which the original stands there as a plain boulder: a card like an obstacle's, 30 by 28, standing 20 texels into its tile. One squat lump of the lava pool's own crust (`60,46,50`, lit `90,70,70` along its upper left, shaded `40,32,40` at its foot and right), broader than it is tall and flat where it sits, split by three cracks that glow as the pool's do: deep red `150,44,30` at a crack's lips, `222,94,36` inside them and `250,158,50` at its heart, with one ember of `255,224,118` where the cracks meet. Nothing of it is white and nothing of it is marked as light: it is a card of the people's shader, bright by its colours alone. No field move clears it and nothing pushes it; it stands in the way until the story takes it.
- **An electric gate** (plan 19 · K4, part b: the two locks across the way to Lt. Surge, which FireRed draws into its tiles) is a card 32 by 30, a whole tile across, standing at its tile's middle: two squat posts of dark steel (`70,72,84`, lit `112,116,130`, a cap of `150,154,168`) at its edges with a yellow lamp `250,214,64` on each cap, and between them three level beams one texel thick, each a white-yellow core `255,246,180` between two rows of `246,206,60`, with a short spark of `255,255,224` where a beam leaves each post. A tile of the gap stands a card each, so a gate two tiles deep is two rows of beams. In the way like the rubble, read, and gone when both switches are found.
- **Things laid out to be taken** (plan 19 · K4, part b), which FireRed stands as objects of their own look and which lay as Poké Balls until then: cards of the item's own size, standing at the tile's middle like an item's ball. **A fossil** (the Dome and the Helix Fossil, 24 by 16) is a flat stone of grey-brown (`168,150,128`, lit `198,184,160`, shade `118,104,92`, edge `82,72,66`) bearing its shell in a darker stone (`132,112,96`): the dome as a half disc ribbed by three arcs, the helix as a spiral of four turns. **The Old Amber** (20 by 20) is a lump of honey `222,150,50` lit `250,206,108` at its upper left, shaded `170,96,34`, with a dark speck `92,52,30` held inside, on a little stand of the museum's wood `120,84,56`. **A Pokédex** on a desk (16 by 12) is a closed red book `206,56,48`, shaded `148,36,38`, with a hinge line `82,24,30` and a small round lens of sky blue `112,190,240`. **The Town Map** on a table (20 by 12) is a rolled sheet of paper `236,228,204` shaded `196,186,160`, tied with a red ribbon `206,56,48`. Each is gone when the story takes it.
- **Rotom's appliances** (plan 20 · J15, part n: the five machines of Silph Co.'s research floor, which HeartGold stands as objects of their own look) are cards a tile across, standing at their tile's middle, each a household machine as a flat front with one darker side and a lit top edge, outlined like every card, and with a little orange-and-pale-blue plug lead at its foot that says it is an appliance Rotom could enter. **The microwave** (30 by 22) is a box of warm white `226,222,212` (lit `244,242,236`, shaded `176,170,160`) with a dark window `48,52,66` at its left holding a warm glow `236,150,70` and a panel of three buttons at its right. **The washing machine** (28 by 30) is a box of the same white with a round door of `96,150,206` ringed in grey `140,140,150` and a strip of controls along its top. **The refrigerator** (24 by 40) is a tall box of pale blue `200,222,236` (lit `226,240,248`, shaded `150,176,196`) cut in two by a line under its upper third, with a handle `120,128,140` on each door. **The fan** (26 by 32) is a round guard of grey `150,156,166` on a stem over a flat foot, with three blades of pale blue `170,206,232` inside and a hub of `226,222,212`. **The mower** (30 by 22) is a body of grass green `86,170,86` (lit `130,204,118`, shaded `56,120,60`) over two black wheels `44,44,52` with a bent handle of grey rising behind it. Nothing of them is marked as light. In the way like a person and spoken to like one: whoever brings a Rotom may send it in.
- **A grotto's hollow** (plan 22 · U11: the mouth of a Hidden Grotto, set in a notch of a tree wall) is a card a tile across, 32 by 30, standing at its tile's middle: a low mound of leaves in two greens (`70,128,62`, lit `104,160,84`, shaded `46,92,48`) with a few tufts standing up from its rim, round a dark opening at its foot (`28,30,26`, deepening to `14,16,14` at its middle) arched by two roots of bark brown (`104,76,50`, lit `138,104,70`), outlined like every card. Nothing of it is marked as light. In the way like a person: the player stands south of it and is asked whether to go in. The grotto inside is Black 2's own room read by the importer, lit as the field is, its one thing standing at the far end on the tile Black 2 keeps for it (a Pokémon as its field sprite, or an item's ball).
- **The Seagallop** (plan 19's last notes: the fast ferry of the Sevii Islands, which FireRed stands at each harbour as an object of its own look) is a card three tiles across, 96 by 44, standing at its tile's middle on the water: a long low hull of white (`236,238,242`, lit `250,250,252`, shaded `188,194,206`) whose bow rises to the left in a point, a band of navy (`38,52,104`) along the waterline and a red stripe (`206,58,52`) over it, and on the deck a cabin of the same white set back from both ends with a row of six dark windows (`44,58,84`, a pale glint `170,206,232` in each top corner) and a short raked mast at its back with a little red flag. Its foot is a dark line of wake (`46,86,140`) with flecks of foam (`226,240,248`) at the stern. Nothing of it is marked as light. In the way like a person; its sailor on the quay is who is spoken to.
- **Celio's network machine** (plan 19's last notes: the machine in One Island's Pokémon Center that joins the islands to Kanto, which FireRed draws into its tiles) is a card two tiles across, 64 by 56, standing at its tile's middle against the back wall: a cabinet of pale grey steel (`196,202,212`, lit `226,230,236`, shaded `150,156,170`) with two screens side by side under its top edge, dark (`32,44,60`) with a line of green readings (`110,214,150`) across each, a band of small lamps under them (red `214,72,66`, amber `232,176,64`, green `110,214,150`, in that order, repeating), and below them a slot of dark grey for the Ruby and the Sapphire with two round sockets. A thick cable of navy (`46,56,96`) runs out of its foot to the right. Nothing of it is marked as light: a card is drawn as people are, opaque. Two of them stand side by side before the block along the room's north-east wall, three tiles apart, so the bank reads as one long machine. In the way like a person; the room's own sign events still say what it is.
- **A rock that is no rock** (plan 02 · S15, part b: the disguise Looker keeps watch in at Stark Mountain) is the cracked rock's own card, unchanged, which is the point of it: a thing of the map that Rock Smash does not break.
- **The swimmer**: whichever Pokémon knows Surf is shown as one round blue swimmer (`86,128,206`, back `138,180,240`, underside `58,88,160`), 48 by 26, seen from the front (two eyes), the back (a tail fin) or the side (the head leading). The rider stands on its back, twelve rows up, and the two bob a texel together.
- **Sinking**: in deep snow and in marsh mud a walker's sprite is lowered by a few rows (4, 7 and 10 for the three depths of snow; 2 and 6 for mud and deep mud) and the ground hides the feet.

### Buildings

A building is a 3D box dressed in pixel art at 32 texels per tile. Every wall face is painted as one piece (a façade, `BuildingArt`): the wall material, a base course, corner posts, then windows, doors, plaques and lanterns placed bay by bay (a bay is one tile, 32 texels). Nothing floats on a wall as a separate decal, so everything sits on the texel grid.

- **Materials**: a flat base colour with one-texel light and dark lines, two or three shades, no per-texel variation.

| Material | Base / light / dark | Drawn as |
|---|---|---|
| Planks | `214,170,118` / `232,194,146` / `150,106,74` | boards 8 texels tall: a light top line, a dark groove, butt joints staggered row by row; one board in five a shade darker |
| Clapboard | `240,236,226` / `252,250,244` / `196,196,210` | boards 6 texels tall with a grey shadow line under each |
| Plaster | `242,232,208` / `250,244,226` / `220,206,180` | flat, with a few short trowel marks three to five texels long |
| Brick | `190,106,84` / `210,130,100` / `156,82,72`, mortar `226,206,184` | courses 4 texels tall, bricks 8 wide, half a brick out of step; whole bricks lighter or darker in a fixed pattern |
| Panel | `214,222,232` / `234,240,246` / `150,162,184` | panels 32×18 with a dark joint and a light line beside it |
| Base course | `158,152,150` / `190,186,182` / `104,100,112` | stone blocks 16×7 with a light top and a dark bottom line |
| Timber posts and beams | `124,84,58` / `156,110,76` / `92,62,48` | four texels wide at the corners of plank walls |
| Boards | `188,144,98` / `210,168,120` / `136,98,70` | the planks' darker boards all over: farmhouses |
| Half-timber | plaster `240,230,206` between beams `110,78,58` / `140,102,74` / `82,58,48` | posts 3 texels wide every 16, a rail at window height, a brace across each end bay |
| Stone | `176,172,170` / `198,194,190` / `124,120,130` | cut blocks 16×8, half a block out of step; one in five a shade darker `160,156,158` |
| Log | `156,110,72` / `188,140,94` / `108,74,56` | logs 8 texels tall, a light line on top and a dark one under each, butt ends every 48 texels staggered row by row |
| Stucco | `244,242,236` / `252,252,248` / `206,208,220` | white and flat, with the plaster's few trowel marks |
| Sheet metal | `150,160,176` / `178,188,200` / `112,122,144` | upright ribs 4 texels wide (light, base, base, dark) and a seam every 32 |
| Dark panel | `104,116,130` / `132,144,156` / `70,80,98` | the panel's joints on a dark ground: Team Galactic's buildings |

- **Roofs**: pitched roofs run their ridge east–west, so the south slope faces the camera almost square-on and its tiles show at full size. Tiles are 8×8 texels in rows half a tile out of step, each with a light upper-left edge, a darker lower right and a dark rounded bottom line; one tile in eleven is a shade lighter. The slope length is a whole number of rows. Eaves overhang 8 texels with a fascia 3 texels deep; gable ends have a pale bargeboard; a ridge cap runs along the top. Centers, Marts and the lab have hip roofs; city blocks have flat roofs behind a parapet, with equipment on top.
- **Walls** under a pitched roof are 56 texels tall; the eave hides the top ten or so, and the 18 texels below the wall's top are painted two flat steps darker (30 % and 15 %) as the eave's shadow. A city block has a ground storey of 44 texels, upper storeys of 32 and a band of 18 along the top that carries its name.
- **Windows**: a white frame 2 texels wide, glass in three flat blues (`170,218,246`, `122,190,236`, `88,156,220`) with one diagonal streak `232,246,255`, mullions 2 texels wide, a sill, and on houses shutters in the roof's colour and a flower box.
- **Doors**: a house door is 22×39 with a pale frame, two rows of sunken panels, a small window and a brass knob, and a wall lantern beside it; public buildings have double glass doors 36×40 in a steel frame, the school a pair of wooden ones. A stone step stands in front of every door. A building the map gives no door (it can't be entered yet) still shows one in a middle bay: shut, its glass dark.
- **Entrance block**: public buildings under a pitched roof (Center, Mart, lab, school) have a block around the door that stands 10 texels proud of the wall and rises to 74, above the eave. Its header, 24 texels tall in the building's accent colour, carries the sign: our own Poké Ball roundel on red for a Center, `MART` on blue, `LAB`, `SCHOOL`, in a 5×7 pixel alphabet of our own drawn at double size with a shadow. City blocks write their name on the band along their top instead.
- **Roof colours** follow the map's roof tile and the building: teal `52,166,138`, red `214,82,66`, blue `70,118,214`; Center `238,104,58`, Mart `66,122,222`, lab `48,178,198`, the school's slate `92,110,156`. Each colour is its own tile texture, so its darks lean violet and its lights warm instead of being one grey texture tinted.
- **Storeys**: a house whose model stands 5.4 tiles tall or more has a second storey: 32 more texels of wall with its own row of windows, under the same roof. A block under a flat roof has one storey for its ground floor and one more for every 1.95 tiles its model stands above 3.8, up to six.
- **Porches**: where the world's data builds the entrance out a tile in front of the wall, so does the building. A *closed* porch is an entrance block as wide as its tiles and 32 texels deep, with the door in its own front and the sign on its header; an *open* one is two side blocks with a canopy between them (its underside 52 texels up), and the door is in the wall behind, so whoever walks in is seen all the way to the door. Under a pitched roof either is 74 texels tall, like the entrance block; under a flat one it is one storey.
- **Wings**: a model whose blocked tiles are more than one rectangle is built as its main block (the one with the way in) and plainer wings of one storey in the same walls, without doors or signs. Pieces one tile thin are a fence where the town builds in wood, a low stone wall elsewhere.

|---|---|---|
| Pokémon Center, Mart, lab | plaster, hip roof (orange, blue, cyan) | the entrance block with its roundel or name |
| Gym | plaster, hip roof in its leader's type: Rock `150,128,100`, Grass `92,170,96`, Ghost `124,98,170`, Fighting `204,96,72`, Water `72,140,214`, Steel `138,150,170`, Ice `126,200,220`, Electric `236,196,64`, and in Unova Poison `150,92,178` (Virbank's club, a small block over its stairs down) and Bug `168,184,64` (Castelia's, Burgh's); Nimbasa's, Elesa's in the amusement park, is Electric's | an entrance block 84 wide in that colour, signed `GYM` |
| Gate house | stone, hip roof green-grey `104,146,122` | an open porch where the road goes through north and south; a door in each end wall where it goes east and west |
| The League's reception gate (Route 22, Johto, plan 20 · J10, part b) | a gate house | one room seen from three roads: a face on Route 26 to the south, on Route 22 to the east and on Route 28 to the west, each built as a gate house of its own |
| Shop (flower shop, cycle shop, café, market, Game Corner, Day Care, Fan Club) | its town's house | an entrance block wide enough for its name, in the roof's colour |
| Department store | panel block of five storeys | its name on the band along the top, ribbon windows |
| Museum | cut stone under a flat roof, two storeys | `MUSEUM` on the band, arched windows |
| Library | brick under a flat roof, three storeys | `LIBRARY` on the band |
| Hotel, restaurant | the Resort's white boards, two storeys | an entrance block with its name |
| Hall (Contest Hall, Pal Park, the Great Marsh's gate, the Battle Frontier's facilities) | plaster, hip roof: Contest magenta `214,92,150`, Pal Park green `96,168,104`, the Frontier's gold `214,170,72` | an entrance block 84 wide with its name |
| Foreign Building (Chapel) | cut stone, two storeys, a steep slate roof `92,104,140` | a round rose window of coloured glass over the door, a small spire on the ridge |
| Temple (Snowpoint) | weathered stone `150,150,160`, flat roofs under snow | two tiers: the wings a storey lower; arched openings between pilasters |
| Shrine (Celestic) | timber | a heavy hip roof `120,74,66` with eaves twice as deep as a house's, no windows |
| Tower (Lost Tower, Battle Tower) | cut stone | four tiers, each a little narrower than the one below, a slit window in each, under a pointed slate cap; in Castelia (Metropolis) a tower is the town's skyscraper, flat on top (the town's row) |
| Lighthouse | white stucco with a red band `214,72,62` | three narrowing tiers, a gallery, and a lantern room whose glass burns all night |
| Factory (the mine's works, the Windworks, the Ironworks) | sheet metal under a flat roof | no windows but a strip under the eave; a sliding door; vents, and a smokestack on the Ironworks |
| Warehouse | sheet metal under a low gable in the same metal; in snow country (Snowpoint's harbour storehouse) logs under a roof of snow | a sliding door two bays wide: in logs, barn doors of boards with rails and a brace across each leaf, and over them on the upper storey a loft door of boards in a frame of beams, with a hoist beam, a rope and a hook above it |
| Mansion | plaster, two storeys, hip roof slate `88,98,126` | arched windows, an entrance block |
| Team Galactic's buildings | dark panel under a flat roof | slit windows `120,230,220`, a yellow band `232,204,76` along the top, spikes on the roof corners |
| Pokémon League | pale stone `214,210,204`, flat roofs | three tiers stepping back, a crimson band `196,60,70`, arched windows, a grand open porch |
| The Indigo Plateau's League (Johto, plan 20 · J10, part b) | pale stone `214,210,204`, two storeys, under a hip roof of league red `176,52,60` | arched windows, glass doors, an entrance block 84 wide signed `LEAGUE` with an accent of old gold `214,178,84`; a lamp at each corner of the plaza before it. Johto's League is one building with its Pokémon Center inside, not Sinnoh's tiers |
| The Ribbon Syndicate (Resort Area) | the Resort's white clapboard, two storeys, under a hip roof of ribbon rose `214,104,150` | a veranda across its whole front where the world builds its entrance out: glazed bays either side of the way in, each a wide window in a white frame, under a canopy in the roof's colour that carries `RIBBONS` between two rosettes (a gold disc `250,210,76` with two tails) |
| The Battleground (Survival Area) | a shop of the Battle Zone: its town's white clapboard under the turquoise hip roof | two small blocks side by side as the world builds it, `BATTLE` on the entrance block of the one with the door |
| The Battle Tower (Battle Frontier; plan 01 · M10, part b) | pale stone `214,210,204` on a flat-roofed ground stage as wide as the world blocks, the Frontier's blue `58,92,170` | a shaft of four stages, the first nine tiles wide and each a seventh narrower than the one under it, a blue band round the top of each and arched windows that burn all night; on the last a gallery and a lantern room, and over that a cap of blue slate half as tall again as a tower's. As tall as its model stands (up to 380 rows): from its door its top is out of the picture, which is the point of it |
| The Battle Hall | pale stone, two storeys, under a hip roof of gold `214,170,72` | arched windows, glass doors, an entrance block with `HALL` |
| The Battle Castle | grey stone, three storeys, a flat roof | battlements (a merlon 8 texels wide every 16, 7 rows above the parapet, along the front and both sides), slit windows, a band of violet `124,98,170`, and at each front corner a turret 30 texels square standing 4 proud of the walls and 28 rows above the parapet under a pointed cap of violet slate |
| The Battle Factory | dark steel panels, three storeys, a flat roof | ribbon windows, a sliding door, a band of orange `232,140,52`, vents and two smokestacks |
| The Battle Arcade | white panel, three storeys, a flat roof | shop windows from end to end, glass doors, a band of magenta `214,92,150`, a skylight and a globe on the roof |
| The lodges beside the Frontier's gate | pale stone, one storey, under a hip roof of the Frontier's blue | shop windows |
| The Battle Tree (Poni Gauntlet, Alola; `BuildingKind.BattleTree`, by the door into `PoniGauntlet_BattleTree`) | logs `156,110,72`, two storeys, under a flat roof the crown hides | a hall grown into a great tree. **The trunk**: a box four bays wide centred on the door, 14 texels proud of the front wall, from the ground up into the crown, in bark: `116,80,54` with plates 10 to 18 texels wide, each with a light line `146,104,70` on its left and a groove `86,58,42` on its right, and here and there a crack across a plate; the door is an arch cut in it (an opening of `70,48,40` with a rim of light bark, a pair of wooden doors and a lit fanlight over them), a board over the arch reading `PARTNERS TREE` (plan 37 · FC3, the approval sheet's 24; the alphabet has no apostrophe) in cream `240,226,190`, and a lantern either side. **Roots**: upright cards 120 by 26 standing just in front of the wall's foot either side of the trunk, three roots of the bark's tones each, thick at the trunk and thinning outward to the ground. **The crown**: leaf clumps in four greens (dark `40,92,56`, base `62,128,68`, light `96,166,84`, tips `146,204,108`), outlined, each clump a disc with a light crescent upper left and a dark one lower right, in three upright cards: a back crown as wide as the building rising 190 rows over its roof, a middle one of 150 rows half way back, and a front fringe of 112 rows standing in front of the wall and the trunk, its foot 18 rows below the top of the wall so the band there is hidden, with vines hanging up to 44 rows below it over the upper windows (two texels wide in the dark green, a three-texel leaf every seven rows to the left and the right in turn, never over the trunk), so that from the field the top of the hall is all leaves. The flat roof under the crown is leaves too. Arched windows that glow after dark |
| The geothermal power plant (Blush Mountain, Alola; `BuildingKind.PowerPlant`, by the door into `BlushMountain_PowerPlant`) | pale panels, two storeys, under a flat roof, a band of plant orange `214,104,52` | `POWER PLANT` on the cornice, ribbon windows, a sliding door. The field looks down at a wall from a few tiles away and a roof is out of its picture, so what says "power plant" stands before the wall. **Pipes**: a run of steel pipe 8 texels thick along the ground storey at 20 rows (`168,176,190`, lit `206,212,222` along its top, shaded `112,120,138`, a rim `92,98,116` along its foot, a flange of that colour every 32 texels), clear of the doors; risers 10 thick every three bays from the ground up the whole wall and bent back over the parapet, never over the name, each with a red valve wheel `196,62,52` 14 texels across where the run meets it; a third run along the roof. **The cooling tower** stands on the ground before the four bays of the west end: an upright card 152 wide and 150 tall, a shell of pale concrete (`200,198,192`, lit `224,222,216` down its west fifth, shaded `150,148,152` down its east third) 140 texels wide at its foot, curving in to 94 a third of the way down and out to 110 at its lip, faint rings `184,182,178` every 16 rows, an orange band near the lip and its dark mouth `92,94,104` seen over it, and above it a still plume of steam (`222,228,236`, shade `188,198,214`, kept off white so it doesn't bloom) drifting east as it rises. **Two steel stacks** 18 texels square and 170 rows tall stand before the three bays of the east end, shaded round, a seam every 24 rows and bands of red and white `236,236,232` ten rows deep at their tops |


- **Lights after dark** are marked in the art itself, texel by texel (their alpha), not found by colour. *Public* lights (street lamps, wall lanterns, Centers, Marts, signs) come on at twilight and stay on all night. *Home* lights (house windows) are on in the evening and off late at night, except in about one house in three. Lit glass keeps its pixel detail: each texel becomes lamplight `255,204,117` scaled by its own brightness.

### Props

**The film studio's poster wall** (plan 42 · PS4, part b; `PropType.PosterWall`): "The film studio", "The posters", below, has its look.

**A photo spot's plate** (plan 43 · LF4; `PropType.PhotoSpot`, `Landmarks.PaintPhotoSpot`): a sprite 26 by 30 texels: a short post in the dark wood and on it a cream plate (236 226 196) with a camera drawn in the wood and a red dot for its lamp, outlined. It blocks nothing; it is read from the tile before it.

- Small things standing outdoors (street lamps, mailboxes, planters, benches, signposts) are **sprites on upright cards**, drawn seen from a little above like the characters, with a one-texel outline in a darker shade of their own colour. They take the scene's light and cast real shadows. A `Table` outdoors is a café's table (plan 23 · Z1, Aquacorde's waterfront): a round cream top with its rim in shade and a cup on it, on a dark iron stem; a room's tables are its furniture.
- **Fences** are real geometry: a post 4 texels square and 17 tall in the middle of every fenced tile, with two rails toward whichever neighbouring tiles are fenced too, so runs, corners and ends come out by themselves. Wood where the houses are timber, white where they are clapboard. Where the world fences a band two tiles thick (the edge of Jubilife City's terrace), the two rows run side by side along the band and are not tied to each other tile after tile: a railing, not a ladder.
- **Items on the ground** (plan 02): an item to pick up lies in a Poké Ball, as in the original, whatever the item is. The ball is a sprite on an upright card like the other props, 18 texels across on a card of 20 by 20 (the outline's texel all round), standing on the middle of its tile: red above (`218,62,58`, a lighter crescent `244,120,104` on its upper left, a darker edge `170,42,56` on its right), white below (`240,240,236`, shaded `198,200,214` on its lower right), a band two texels high across its middle (`58,52,72`) with a round button four texels across on it, white in a ring of the band's colour. It takes the scene's light, casts a shadow and has a dark patch under it, a little over half a person's. It is in the way like a person, and it is gone the moment it is picked up. A hidden item shows nothing at all.
- **Street lamps** are 78 texels tall: an iron post on a stepped foot with a four-sided lantern. After dark the lantern glows, a soft halo sits around it and a round pool of light lies at its foot. A lamp stands on the ground round it, never on a square of its own: a single tile of "fence" by itself in a paved street is one of Jubilife City's lamps, which the import can't tell from a post.
- Towns are furnished through the map data (`Fence`, `LampPost`, `Mailbox`, `Planter`, and `Bench`, which outdoors is a park bench): a fenced front garden with the mailbox at its gate, lamps beside the roads, planters either side of public doors, a bench where there is something to look at. In the imported world the lamps stand where the original's do, and the rest of what stands about comes from its models:

| Prop | Look |
|---|---|
| Low wall | where a fence would be in a town of brick or stone: a wall 12 texels tall and 8 thick in cut stone with a pale cap, joining up tile to tile like a fence |
| Fountain | a round basin of pale stone, 3 tiles across, its water in the pond's blues with two ripple rings, and a jet on a pedestal in the middle: a sprite of white and pale-blue streaks 40 texels tall |
| Boat | a white hull with a blue band along the waterline, a planked deck, a cabin with a row of windows and a red funnel; it lies the way its model is longer |
| Wind turbine | a white tower tapering from 10 texels to 6, as tall as its model, a nacelle, and three blades 44 texels long (a sprite; they turn in plan 04 · G9) |
| Statue | a stepped plinth of cut stone carrying a weathered figure in green bronze `98,150,138` (ours: a long-necked creature rearing on its hind legs) |
| Honey tree | a broad round crown in a warmer, yellower green than the forest's (`150,190,84`, `196,220,104`, `104,150,70`) on a thick trunk with an amber patch of honey `236,176,64` |
| Crates | two wooden crates and one on top: boards `188,134,84` with dark frames |
| Coal heap | a mound in three flat darks `74,72,84`, `54,52,66`, `104,102,112`, with two-texel glints |
| Hedge | a clipped box of leaves in the planter's greens, as large as its model's tiles; white on top where its town lies under snow |
| Column | a fluted shaft of pale stone on a square base, as tall as its model; broken ones end in a slanted break |
| Topiary | a cone of clipped leaves on a short trunk |
| Cairn | three worked stones one on another: the Hallowed Tower, a stone tablet |
| Billboard | a board 44 texels wide on two posts, pale with three dark lines |
| Outcrop | a mass of rock as large as its tiles, in the boulders' three shades with ledges every 10 texels |
| Mast | a lattice mast as tall as its model, a red light on top that burns all night |
| Prism tower | Lumiose City's tower of light (`PropType.PrismTower`, plan 23 · Z2; our own drawing, redrawn in Z2's second part because from the field's steep camera the first, twelve tiles of dark lattice, read as a black block running off the top of the picture): four stages stacked on its four tiles by four, each narrower and shorter than the one under it (128, 100, 72 and 44 texels across; 40, 34, 28 and 20 tall), so that the steep camera sees a stepped pyramid of bright decks with a short wall under each; the walls are lit glass `176,224,244` between pale steel mullions `214,222,236` with a band of gold light `250,226,150` under each deck, the decks pale steel `226,230,240` with a lit gold rim, then a gold crown and a short spire; about four and a half tiles high in all (146 texels), so the whole tower stands in the picture with the player a tile or two from its foot; everything lit burns all night; it blocks its sixteen tiles |
| Drums | three steel drums, two before and one behind: blue `78,112,168`, rust `170,96,64` and blue, each with a lid seen from above and two dark bands |
| Ore heap | a mound like the coal heap's in the rust of iron ore: `150,96,78`, `118,72,62`, `186,132,108`, with grey glints (Iron Island) |
