| Headframe | the steel tower of a lift or over a mine's shaft, as tall as its model: two lattice legs leaning in to a platform, a cross-brace every 16 rows, and the winding wheel on top, a ring of steel with four spokes (Iron Island's lifts) |
| Lift base | a steel platform knee high, ribbed on top, with a black and yellow edge |
| Drawbridge leaf | over the planks the ground already shows: a steel truss along either edge of the deck, 16 rows tall, and at the bank end a portal of two posts and a beam 64 rows up, with a counterweight hanging from it (Canalave City) |
| Marsh tram | the Great Marsh's little tram on its rails: two steel rails on sleepers the length of its tiles, and a car of green boards with an open side, a pale roof and wheels, at the end nearer the gate |
| Binoculars | a coin viewer on a post: a grey head with two eyepieces on a column 34 texels tall |
| Pavilion | a white open shelter of the Hotel Grand Lake: four posts and a turquoise roof in the Resort's colours |
| Sea stack | a stack of rock in the sea (Sunyshore City), built like the relief's rock: a wide foot wet where the waves break on it (`96,98,118`, `70,72,92`), a body over its west side and a taller crown over its east, each set back from the one under it, and a boulder broken off at the foot. The tops are mottled rock `158,152,150` / `146,140,140` with cracks `118,112,120` and gulls' white; the crown is grown over with sea grass `120,190,104` (tufts `76,146,88`, a ragged edge) and clumps of pink flowers `232,150,186`. The water's foam laps round its foot |
| Snowdrift | a long low heap of snow banked against a wall, in the roof's snow `226,234,246`, `204,216,238` and its blue shade `176,192,226`, with drift lines along it, outlined in the deepest |
| Rift | a tear standing in the air at Spear Pillar: a jagged slit 36 texels wide and as tall as its model, its core `48,34,66`, its edge lit (blue for Dialga's, pink for Palkia's) and glowing all night, with sparks round it |
| Rift's shadow | the dark the rifts cast on the floor: rings of violet falling to the dark of the rift at the middle, flat on the ground, and a wisp rising from it |
| Container | a shipping container on the Fight Area's quay (plan 01 · M10), as large as its model's tiles (three by three) and 62 rows tall: corrugated steel, a rib every 4 texels, in one of four colours by where it stands (rust `176,84,62`, sea green `62,138,128`, ochre `206,160,70`, slate blue `84,110,156`), in a frame of the same paint a shade darker with a steel casting at each corner. Its south end has a pair of doors, each with two locking bars from top to bottom and a handle, and white stencilled letters on a darker patch (the quay's and the colour's: the sign alphabet has no figures); its roof is ribbed across, lit along its south and west edges |
| Grand fountain | the fountains in the two pools before the Battle Frontier's gate (the Fight Area): a square plinth of cut stone three tiles across and 10 rows high standing in the pool's water (the three tiles by three the world blocks in the middle of the pool), a second step of pale stone two thirds as wide and 8 rows higher, and on it the town fountain's round basin and jet, whose rings and drops move in the four frames of everything that moves in place |
| Pool | a swimming pool of the Resort Area, as large as its model's tiles and set in paving, so no lawn shows through it: a kerb of pale tiles `224,226,222` 6 texels tall and 6 wide round water in the pond's blues, a darker band along its north and west sides where the kerb shades it, three lane lines of pale dashes, a steel ladder near its south-east corner, and on its north-east corner a parasol in the Resort's turquoise and white, eight gores on a white pole, over a deckchair of white slats. Whoever stands in it (the Resort Area's swimmer) stands on its floor, ten rows of their sprite under the water |
| Lava | a pool of lava on Stark Mountain's flank (plan 01 · M10, part b), as large as its model's tiles, lying two texels above rock the builder has made level under it, so the rock round it stands as its rim: plates of dark crust `60,46,50` with a paler edge `90,70,70`, about 18 texels across, and between them cracks that glow from deep red `150,44,30` at a plate's edge through `222,94,36` and `250,158,50` to yellow `255,224,118` in the middle, inside a ragged rim of scorched rock `76,64,70`. The glow is marked as light. In the four frames of everything that moves in place the heat travels from crack to crack and a few bubbles open; the plates stay where they are |
| Crater | the mouth of Stark Mountain: three tiers of basalt (the relief's rock face mixed halfway to `70,62,72`) stepping in and up from the plateau the world blocks under its model, 120 to 210 rows in all, with short seams of heat glowing in their faces; the uppermost holds the crater, a ring of crust round a throat that glows red at its lip to yellow in its middle, and over it a column of smoke in three greys (`158,150,152`, `120,112,118`, `84,76,86`), five puffs that grow and drift east as they rise, lit warm at the throat, climbing a quarter of a puff's way each of the four frames. It stands east of the way into the mountain and north of that way's row, so the door stays in plain sight |
| Booth | a stall of the Battle Frontier's Exchange Service Corner, three tiles each way, round its attendant: a back wall of pale boards 50 rows tall with three shelves of prizes, under an awning in broad stripes of blue `58,108,214` and white with a scalloped edge, and a counter 20 rows tall along its other three sides (a top of polished wood, a front of pale panels), so the attendant is seen from the waist up. The side the world marks as a counter carries a price board |
| Scale model | the model of the Battle Frontier in the middle of its plaza: a table of pale stone four tiles each way and 16 rows tall, its top the grounds in miniature (lawn, the cross of paved ways, two pools, the four halls as blocks in their own colours), and the tower a block of pale stone under a blue cap standing 28 rows above it |
| Trainer statue | the two bronzes before the Battle Frontier's gate, two tiles each way: a stepped plinth of stone 24 rows tall with a gold plate, and on it a trainer in warm bronze `170,126,78`, one arm thrown up with a Poké Ball in the hand, the other on the hip |
| Colossus | the statue in Snowpoint Temple's hall: a plinth of cold stone `150,160,178` across its tiles and on it a giant 92 rows tall, a barrel of a body on short legs with shoulders wider than the plinth and arms to the knee, a row of seven hollows across its face, bands cut round wrists and ankles, and rime `206,228,244` along everything that faces up |

**The mine** (Oreburgh's yard and the mine under it; plan 01 · M5). The model of the yard blocks thin pieces of ground between its sheds, and those carry its conveyors (`WorldMapBuilder.Conveyors`):

- **A conveyor** is a steel deck 20 texels wide, its top 68 rows above the ground and its underside 62, clear of whoever walks beneath (a character is 58). The belt on it is dark rubber `58,56,68` between steel edges, with a joint `40,38,50` every 16 texels and a lump of coal `30,30,40` between each pair of joints; belt and coal move four texels a frame, in the four frames of everything that moves in place.
- **A straight run of three blocked pieces or more** carries a belt along itself, on a **pier** under each tile: a lattice 16 texels square, two steel posts with a rung every 16 rows and braces crossing between, the air between them cut out.
- **Two pieces facing each other across one open tile** are a **gantry**: two piers and a beam across under the belt. The belt passes between them, across the pair, and runs on over the open ground either way until something blocked takes it up (a shed, a heap of coal, a run that carries it on), if that is within eight tiles. One walks under it.
- **A piece by itself** is a pier with a steel cap.
- Steel is `118,128,150`, lit `160,170,190`, shaded `78,86,110`. The sheds are the factory kind of building (ribbed metal).

**Amity Square** (plan 06 · R16c, `Landmarks.Amity.cs`):

- **A hut of the ruins** is walked into, so it is built and not a sprite: three walls of great blocks laid dry (courses 13 to 18 rows tall, blocks 24 to 41 texels long, each a little its own tone of the stone `160,156,158`, lit `200,196,192` along its top and left, seams `76,72,90`), 68 rows tall, under one slab 12 rows thick that stands four texels proud of them. Together they are well over a character's 58 rows: whoever steps through the doorway is under the slab and not seen until they come out, here or out of the hut it leads to. The doorway is in the south face over the one open tile of the hut's south row: 26 texels wide and 50 rows tall, the dark of the room `30,28,42` between two uprights seven texels wide and under a lintel ten rows deep, with daylight reaching nine rows in over the sill. The hut is five tiles wide, the room's three and a wall's either side. Moss at the walls' feet and lichen on the slab, in greens taken from the lawn.
- **A receptionist's desk** is a counter of the Battle Frontier's booths (wooden top, pale stone front, 20 rows tall) down the tile she serves over, with a return at each end: the tile she stands on is left open, and she is seen from the waist up behind it.

### Rooms

- A room is a doll's house: floor, a back wall 80 texels tall and two side walls, cut away at the front. The wall strip is crown moulding, wallpaper with a small repeating motif, a chair rail, panelled wainscot and a skirting board.
- **A room rebuilt to the original's plan** keeps the original's tiles, so its people stand where the original's do: its floor begins where the original's does (`Map.RoomCorner`), with as much wall behind and beside it as the plan has, and the way out is the row below the original's exit mat. Its inner walls (wall tiles inside the floor: the Valley Windworks' hall and the corridor below it) are cut away as the front wall is, but only down to the wainscot: 28 texels of wainscot and skirting on every face that looks onto the floor, under a cap in the room's own colours: the wainscot's base in shade by a fifth, with a rail of the room's trim two texels wide (its light texel outermost) only along those faces, so a thick wall reads as one ledge. Only the frame of the doll's house (the cut tops of its outer walls, the front wall's kerb, and an inner wall with nothing to the south of it, which is the room's front there) keeps the dark top `62,56,74`: inside the room that dark read as a slab of navy furniture. Cut lower, they would read as a pit; at full height, they hid the hall behind them. A gate house's doors are in its side walls (the gate to Route 209): each is part of its wall, and the way out is the floor tile inside it, marked with the same mat as a front door's, its arrow pointing at the wall. The Lost Tower's floors (plan 02 · S7) keep the original's graves: a headstone is an upright card a tile wide, weathered grey stone `150,150,158` with a rounded head, a darker rim and one carved line; a tomb is a card two tiles wide, a broad stone on a plinth with a niche of flowers at its foot. Its stairs are the Galactic building's turned round: a flight climbing east from its warp, a stairwell going down west.
- **The friends' hall** (plan 13 · V23): Jubilife City's Pokémon Center upstairs as plan 01 · M11 built it, nothing added to its floor or walls. The friends' trainers stand south of the link desks in two rows of four (tiles 5, 8, 11 and 14 of rows 7 and 9), facing south toward whoever comes up the stairs, each drawn as the player's own looks are (the boy's or the girl's, by their file), with nothing over their head; the room's own three attendants stay behind their desks. With no files in `trainers/` the floor is as it was.
- **A restaurant** (plan 37 · FC5: Lumiose's four, the Bistro des Deux, La Table à Trois, Le Carrousel and La Grande Table) is a house's room fifteen tiles by twelve with nothing new in its kit: the kitchen's pass (a `Counter` five tiles long) along the middle of the back wall with a `Stove` and a `KitchenCounter` beside it and a plant at each end, two windows and a clock on the back wall, four tables of two tiles square, two each side of an aisle from the door to the host, who stands before the pass, and a rug down the aisle. The staff the courses are battled against stand beside the aisle and by the stove, two diners at the tables; the four differ by their people alone, never by a colour or a piece of furniture of their own.
- **Floors**: planks 8 texels wide in three wood tones (whole planks, never single texels) with dark grooves and staggered joints; or tiles 16 texels square in two close tones with a grout line and a light edge. Walls shade the floor beside them in two flat steps.
- **Furniture** is built from boxes whose every visible face is painted: a light line where the top meets the front, a dark line at the foot, panels and drawers as sunken or raised bevels, handles and knobs of two texels or more. Plants and vases are sprites on cards.
- **A seat is walked onto** (plan 01 · M11). The original's stools and floor cushions block nobody: whoever sits at a table stands on the seat's own tile, and the player may walk over an empty one. So a seat (`PropType.Cushion`) is no chair: it is a square cushion 20 texels across and 5 high in the sofa's red (`214,96,84`), a light line where its top meets its front, a seam two texels in from its edge and a button at its middle. It lies on the floor as a rug does and never blocks; a person on its tile reads as sitting there. A `Chair`, with its back and legs, stays for the rooms made by hand, where nobody sits on one.
- **Partitions of their own** (plan 20 · J15, part s; `PartitionLook`, `Graphics/PartitionArt.cs`): an imported room whose blocked tiles stand for something other than wall names it in its catalogue (`WorldModels.PartitionsOf`, by the region and the room's map), and each of its inner-wall tiles is built as that, a box a tile at a time whose top has a one-texel rim along the sides that look onto the floor, so a run reads as one piece, never as the back wall come forward. *Hedges* (the Goldenrod Gym's maze), 24 texels: leaves in clumps of four texels in three greens `68,136,66` to `88,160,78`, now and then a pink `244,140,176` or white flower on the top, over a planter of cream stone eight texels deep. *Counters* (the tunnel's stalls, Buena's floor, Silph Co.'s lobby), 22: a pale laminate top `236,228,208` with a lip, pale wood panels `204,160,114` a panel to every 16 texels, a dark kick `110,84,64`. *Bulkheads* (the S.S. Aqua's decks, the tunnel's service ways, and Hoenn's S.S. Tidal, plan 21 · part q, whose cabins' walls had stood their full height as the back wall come forward and hid the cabins), 28: ship's grey plate `120,130,144` in panels of 16 with a row of rivets under the top, a band of black and yellow at the foot, a top of steel plate with rivets along its seams and a yellow rim. *Rock* (the Cianwood Gym), 22: rough grey stone darker toward its foot, the top lit at its upper left. *Ice* (the Mahogany Gym), 24: pale blue `162,206,236` with streaks and a white top edge, a diagonal sheen on the top. *Pillars* (the Glitter Lighthouse's floors), 44 and set four texels in, each a tile alone: fluted pale stone `214,208,192` between a capital and a base. A tile with no floor within two tiles of it is the solid between the room's halls, not a partition: the room's dark `30,26,38` at the floor's level, as beyond a house's plan, so no hall of floor shows where nobody walks. Since plan 20 · J15, part u six more, and a stretch of a room may have a look other than the rest of it (`PartitionPatch`, `WorldModels.PartitionPatchesOf`; beside a patch each side shows its face): *Tables* (the S.S. Aqua's mess, a patch among the hold's bulkheads), 18: planks of dark wood `140,96,64` with a seam every eight texels, a top's edge four texels deep over a shade with a stout leg at each end. *Crates* (the Department Store's basement), 26: two crates to a tile, lids of pale slats `204,164,110` framed `132,96,58`, sides of planks with a cross brace and now and then a stencilled mark. *Consoles* (the radio towers' studios and offices, the Power Plant, Bill's cottage), 24: a desk of cream steel `222,216,198` with a dark panel `58,64,78` of red, green and yellow buttons in its back half, a dark band of lit buttons under the face's top, a kick at the foot. *Cases* (the Pewter Museum, the House of Memories), 24: glass over a bed of deep teal cloth `60,94,102` with a pale stone laid in the middle and one sheen across, a brass-edged front over a plinth of dark wood. *Lattices* (Ecruteak's halls and dance theatre), 28: paper panels `240,234,214` in a grid of dark timber `84,56,38` every eight texels, a deep rail at the top and the foot. *Fences* (the Moomoo Farm's stable), 18: two rails of weathered wood between posts, straw at their foot, a top of packed earth `124,100,74` under the rail. The rest of the rooms part s left are counters (the stores, the gates, the stations, the halls of counters and desks), hedges (the reception gate, the roofs, the flower shop) or bulkheads (Team Rocket's hideout, the S.S. Aqua's cabins, the lighthouse's lamp room). Since part v the gates of twelve by twelve tiles have a planter bed (hedges) for the block between their two front alcoves, the charcoal kiln two patches of crates in its corners (its back wall come forward stays wall), and Mahogany's souvenir shop counters. **A ship's engines** (the S.S. Aqua's hold): a casing of green-grey steel `96,118,108` 40 texels tall on each tile the engines' model blocks, its sides only where no engine stands beside it, with a grille of vents, a gauge and a black and yellow plinth, a cylinder head 16 texels square and 10 tall with a red cap on every tile, and a brass pipe along the row. **People in a room stand straight on screen** while the room keeps its perspective (`FieldShaders.SetUprightCards`): a card at the picture's edge leaned with the walls, and a card seen side on read as someone falling over.
- **A way on through a house's back wall** (plan 01 · M11: the Old Chateau's doors, a lift's) is painted on the wall over the tile that leads on, as Team Galactic's is, in the room's own materials. *A doorway*: the dark of the room beyond `30,24,30`, 22 by 47 texels, two steps of shade at its head and jambs, in a frame of dark wood (`128,86,56`) three texels wide with a lintel five deep. *A lift's doors* (the tile's behaviour is the original's `Door`): two leaves of brushed steel (`196,200,210`) shut on a dark seam, in a steel frame, with a lit call button `250,214,76` beside the right jamb and a floor light over the head. A way through an inner wall (the Old Chateau's dining room, under its gallery) is the gap the plan leaves in that wall, with a mat in it. A way through the back wall come forward (plan 01 · M11, part b: the Pokémon Mansion's corridors, whose own back wall stands three tiles before its hall's) is the same doorway painted on that wall's face over the tile that leads on: the tile is wall, stands the wall's full height with the rest, and whoever takes the door walks into it. **A house whose plan is no rectangle** (the same mansion: a corridor the width of the house crossing a hall) has nothing where its plan has nothing: beyond the one tile of wall that bounds its floor, and before that floor, the room's tiles are void, drawn as the dark outside every room is, never as a slab of wall top the size of the house. The wall with nothing beyond it to the south is the room's front there, cut down to the same low kerb as the front wall, so whoever walks along it is seen whole; a side wall stops where the void beside it begins. **A laboratory's room whose plan is no rectangle** (plan 01 · M11, the last step: Professor Rowan's lab, a wide hall at the back and the length of the lab before its west half) follows the same rule for void that its own walls shut off from its floor. Void that the floor runs up to is not outside the plan but a well inside the room (Pal Park's gallery, open over its lobby): the room's walls and front kerb stay whole round it.
- **Stairs at the side of a house's room** are the Galactic building's flights in the house's own wood (`214,160,104` treads, `184,126,78` risers, a rail and newel of `128,86,56`); anywhere else they stay steel. **A block of wall that reaches the back wall** of a house with nothing but wall behind it (the flank of such a staircase) is the back wall come forward: it stands the wall's full height, its top the wall's paper cut through: the paper's colour in shade by a third, with the trim's two-texel rail along the sides that look onto the floor, never the frame's dark (a staircase's flank under the dark read as a navy slab over the room), where an inner wall with floor behind it is cut down to the wainscot so the camera sees over it.
- **A house on two levels** (the Old Chateau's hall, whose gallery runs round three sides three tiles up; plan 01 · M11) is drawn with relief as a Gym's tiers are, and the faces its upper floor stands on are the room's own wainscot under its chair rail (`GroundBaker.RoomFace`: a panel to every 16 texels across and 24 down), never the field's earth. Its stairs are the floor's own planks on the slope the plan gives; the rails round the gallery are inner walls cut to the wainscot, standing at the gallery's height.
- **A flower stand** (`PropType.FlowerStand`, plan 01 · M11: the Floaroma flower shop, the Berry Master's house) is an upright card on each tile it covers: two wooden steps, three clay pots on the lower and two on the upper, each with a tuft of leaf and a bloom four texels across in one of six colours (red `226,72,72`, yellow `250,214,76`, pink `240,140,180`, white `246,246,250`, blue `110,150,230`, orange `240,150,70`), dealt round by the tile so a row of stands is never one colour. The room's own tiles say that it blocks.
- **An escalator** (plan 01 · M11, part d: the Department Store's, the Pokémon Centers', Pal Park's) is for now the side stairs in steel, a flight beside its warp, taken as stairs are. A stairwell one tile wide (the Centers' way down) has steps four texels wide: the shade lines across a tread would be single texels there, so its treads are plain, each darker than the one above it under a lit nosing. No texel stands alone.
- **A vending machine** (`PropType.VendingMachine`, plan 01 · M11, part d: the two on the roof of Veilstone's Department Store) is a box 26 texels wide, 20 deep and 60 high, a little taller than a fridge, in the store's blue: a window in the upper half of its front with three rows of cans in three colours (the water's blue, the soda's green, the lemonade's yellow), a row of three buttons under it and a dark flap at the foot where the can drops. The window is glass that lights after dark (`ArtSheet.PublicLight`), its frame painted over it afterwards. It stands with its back to a wall and is read from the tile before it; like the decorations after `Rug` it blocks nothing by itself: the room's own tiles do.
- **A wall of shelves** (`PropType.WallShelf`, plan 01 · M11, the last rooms: the back wall of a Poké Mart behind the open floor of its east end, where the original's customers stand looking at the goods) is the store shelf's own front hung on the back wall over the tiles it covers: the steel frame, three boards and the goods in the shelf's six colours, 44 texels high from the floor and six texels proud of the wall under a steel top. It lies on the wall's row as a painting does and blocks nothing: the floor before it is walked.
- **A blackboard** (`PropType.Blackboard`, plan 01 · M11, the last rooms: the Trainers' School) hangs on the back wall over the tiles it is read from: a board of dark green (`46,92,74`) 30 texels high, its foot 36 above the floor, in a frame of wood (`150,104,66`) two texels wide with a ledge four deep under it, and on it three rows of chalk (`226,232,220`) two texels high in runs of six to fourteen with gaps of four. It lies on the wall's row as a painting does and blocks nothing; whoever stands before it reads it.
- **A pile of books** (`PropType.BookPile`, plan 01 · M11, part c: the houses of Sunyshore's inventor, of the Survival Area's black belt and of the old gentleman of Route 228, whose piles are read) is an upright card on its tile, 26 texels wide and 24 high: five books lying flat one on another, each four texels thick and 18 to 24 long, in the bookshelf's own cover colours (red `200,66,66`, blue `66,114,200`, green `76,160,96`, yellow `230,190,76`, violet `150,96,176`), a lighter line along the top of each cover, a darker one along its foot, and the cream of its pages (`236,228,208`) three texels wide at one end. The books are shifted a texel or two against each other, and which colour lies where and which end the pages show at go by the tile, so two piles in a room are never the same. The room's own tiles say that it blocks.
- **A workbench** (`PropType.Workbench`, plan 39 · CR1: the craftswoman's workshop in Sunyshore City; `WorkshopArt`) is built as a table is: a top of planks 5 texels thick at 21 to 26 high on two trestles (legs `128,86,56` with a crossbar at 7 to 10), the top of pale wood (`214,160,104`) with a seam every 14 texels, a few dark nicks, and on it from the left a heap of shavings (cream `240,222,178`), a mallet (a dark head on a haft), three chisels side by side (steel blades, red handles `200,66,66`) and at the right end an iron vice (`110,116,132`, its jaws 9 high, its handle in front); a small coil of cord hangs on the near trestle. Any width in whole tiles, one deep; the room's own tiles say that it blocks.
- **A training hall** (plan 36 · TR2; `SunyshoreTrainingHall`, `TrainingArt`): one plan of ours, 15 tiles by 12, a house's room (planks, wallpaper, wainscot) with three rows of back wall. At the back on the left the court: three drill targets on the back wall over tiles 2, 4 and 6 and a training mat before them, six tiles by four. At the back on the right the head coach and the clerk behind a counter four tiles long (tiles 10 to 13, the room's `Counter` behaviour so they are spoken to across it), and between them since plan 36 · TR9 the tutor of the later games' moves (tile 12), a wall of shelves behind them and crates closing the counter's end; a bench by the west wall for the teacher (TR8), a plant in the east corner for the keeper (TR5), and the mat inside the door. *A drill target* (`PropType.DrillTarget`) is a card on the back wall 28 texels square with its foot 30 over the floor: a raised frame of pale wood (`196,146,96`) round a board of `226,214,192`, on it a round target 23 texels across in rings, a dark rim `120,96,74` outermost, cream `250,242,222`, a rim, blue `70,120,210`, a rim and the club's orange `232,112,64` in the middle, with two dark nicks where shots have struck. *A training mat* (`PropType.TrainingMat`) lies on the floor as a rug does, two texels in from its tiles' edges: padded blue in panels 32 texels square of two blues (`84,132,200`, `92,142,210`) with seams of `64,104,168`, a darker edge `52,86,146`, a white border two texels wide three in, and a white lane line across its middle. Neither blocks: the mat is walked on, the targets hang on the wall.
- **A lift with an attendant** (the Ribbon Syndicate's, plan 01 · M11, part c) is the same car as the lighthouse's with someone standing in its corner by the door: the doors in each floor's back wall lead into it, she asks for the floor, the ride is every lift's ("Machines at work", below), and the car's own door then leads out onto the floor it has come to.
- **A covered street** (`InteriorStyle.Street`, plan 22, wave 48: Join Avenue, under its glass roof) is no house's room: its floor is the ground its tiles say, the street's paving, as a Gym's is, with pale flagstones `226,220,204` / `212,204,186` (tiles 16 square, grout and light edge as every tiled floor) where a tile is plain floor; its walls and the blocks of its shops between the street's lanes are shopfronts, cream stucco `240,230,208` with a coral motif and trim `226,104,92` over a band of shop glass `110,168,196` and a slate skirting `72,86,110`.
- **A wooden tower** (`InteriorStyle.Tower`, plan 20, wave 77: Sprout Tower's three floors) is no house's room. Its floor is dark polished boards 8 texels deep running east and west, joints staggered, in three tones (`136,90,54` / `148,98,60` / `160,108,68`, grooves `92,60,38`). Its walls are paper screens (`236,226,200`) in a lattice of timber `128,86,54` two texels wide every 16 across and 14 down, over a wainscot of dark timber `110,74,48` and a skirting `70,48,34`. Where the original leaves the floor (the gaps between its ring and its hall, the openings where the pillar rises through the floors above) the room has nothing: those tiles are void (an overlay's `overTheDark`), and every floor stands on timber two tiles deep over the dark (`MapScene.WellDepth`), whatever the floor beside it does: the face (`BuiltArt.TimberFace`) is a beam along its head (`120,82,52`, lit `156,112,74`, shaded `80,54,34`) and below it a post six texels wide at each tile's west end with a cross beam three rows deep between the posts at the head of every 48 rows, the dark `30,24,26` between them. Along every edge of a floor that ends over the dark stands a **rail** of the same timber, a texel in from the edge, 18 texels high: a post four texels square at each end of the tile's edge, a bar three deep along its head and a bar two deep half way up. A tile that is a way somewhere has none, so no rail stands across a flight of stairs. The walls stand over the dark with nothing under them. **The pillar** (`GymPieces`, over the tiles of the room's columns, three by three) is one trunk of dark old timber `104,70,46` (lit west `132,92,60`, shaded east `70,46,30`), its faces ten texels in from the edge of its tiles, from the dark two tiles under the floor to 104 texels over it: grain in streaks a texel wide, a band of dark iron `52,50,58` two texels deep every 40 rows with a rivet `120,118,128` every 8 across, and a rope of straw `226,206,150` four texels deep round it 18 texels over its foot. Its top is the trunk's end, rings of its grain round a dark heart. It **sways**: it leans east and west on its foot, at most 0.022 of a radian, once every 3.6 seconds of the field's clock, as the pagoda's pillar does. It stands in the way as the columns did: the room's own tiles block.
- **Walkways over the dark** (`InteriorStyle.Aerie`, plan 20, wave 77: the Violet Gym, Falkner's) are a wooden tower's floors and rails in pale boards running north and south, the way the walkways lead to the Leader (`202,174,128` / `214,186,140` / `224,198,152`, grooves `158,126,88`), with the same timber under them, the same rails and the same dark where the original leaves the floor. The walls are the sky `196,222,244` with small white clouds `246,250,255`, six texels wide, on the wallpaper's own grid, a white rail and crown over a wainscot of timber `150,108,70`. Where the walkways' level stands over the entrance's floor the face between them is the same timber, as tall as the drop.
- **A Battle Tent** (`InteriorStyle.Tent`, plan 21, wave of the Hoenn look: the lobbies, corridors and battle rooms of Verdanturf's, Fallarbor's and Slateport's tents, by their overlays' `interior`) is canvas, not a house. Its floor is packed sand `222,198,154` / `214,188,142` with a scatter of darker grains `184,156,112`, one texel each, about one in forty, placed by the texel's own seed. Its walls are the tent's canvas in upright stripes sixteen texels wide, red `200,70,60` and cream `242,228,198`, a rope-coloured crown and rail `214,180,120`, over a hem of deep red canvas `150,50,44`. A floor of ice in any room of planks or tiles (the Trick House's eighth puzzle) shows the field's own ice where its tiles are ice.
- **A Gym's hall over the dark** (plan 22, wave 48: the Nimbasa Gym's runway) has nothing where the original draws nothing round its walks: those tiles are void, as under the Sunyshore Gym's gears, so the runway and its stage stand lit on steel faces (`SceneTextures.PowerFace`) over the dark instead of in a slab of wall top.
- **A bedroom** (plan 02 · S4) has a bed and a desk with a computer. The bed is two tiles long against the north wall: a dark wooden headboard 26 texels tall, a frame 14 tall, a cream pillow at the head, the sheet turned back over a blue blanket quilted in lines eight texels apart. The desk is the PC's (`PropModels.BuildPc`): wood with a grille on its top and a monitor whose screen shows three lines of text.
- **Rooms follow the clock**: by day the windows show sky and throw a soft patch of light across the floor, slanting the way the shadows fall; at twilight the glass and the patches turn orange; at night the glass is dark blue (`18,26,56`), the patches are gone and the room is lit warm and a little dimmer by its lamps (`ArtLook.IndoorRigFor`).
- **Team Galactic's rooms** (the Eterna building, the Veilstone warehouse and the headquarters behind it; plan 02 · S6, S8, S11) are the lab's: pale tiles, grey-blue wainscot, racks of machines, cardboard boxes. What is Team Galactic's own in them takes the colours of their buildings outside ("Buildings": the dark panel `104,116,130` / `132,144,156` / `70,80,98`, the teal of the slit windows `120,230,220`, the yellow band `232,204,76`), so a floor of the headquarters reads as the inside of the block it is.
  - *A warp panel* is a plate of the dark panel 28 texels square set into the floor and a texel proud of it, bevelled, with a ring of teal light 20 across and two wide, a core of the same teal 8 across (its middle a paler `204,250,244`), and a yellow tick three texels long at the middle of each side. The panel that leads out of the building, and the pad on the ground floor it lands on, have the ring and the core in green `110,220,120` (middle `206,250,206`) instead: the green one is the way out. A pad that carries nobody is drawn the same as one that does.
  - *Riding a panel*: whoever ends a step on one turns on the spot, a quarter turn every fourteenth of a second, for half a second; the picture closes as any way through does; and on the far panel they turn on for three tenths of a second and come to rest facing as they did when they stepped on. The field waits throughout.
  - *A locked door* is a shutter across a gap two tiles wide in an inner wall, a leaf to each tile and as high as the wall is cut (28 texels), so it closes the wall it stands in: slats of the dark panel four texels deep, the yellow band four texels deep along its top, and a slit of teal light at its middle, 10 by 2. It stands in the way like a person and is read like one; unlocked, both leaves are gone with the door's sound, for good.
  - *A doorway on* through a back wall (the laboratory's, to the control room; any room that is no Gym) is painted on the wall over the tile behind the floor that leads on: the dark of the room beyond `20,18,28`, 22 by 47 texels, with two steps of shade at its head and jambs, in a frame of the dark panel three texels wide with the yellow band across its head. Whoever takes it walks into the wall there and is gone behind it.
  - *A tank* of the laboratory stands on two tiles by two: a steel foot and a steel cap `150,160,176`, and between them a cylinder of glass 52 texels across and 46 high seen as three faces, the fluid inside green `96,206,140` (lighter `150,232,178` down its left, deeper `58,150,104` at its right and foot), a darker shape `40,104,80` hanging in the middle of it and a few bubbles of `206,250,226`. Nothing of it is white.
  - *A containment unit* of the control room holds one of the lakes' Pokémon, which stands in the field between two racks with a third behind it: the racks are the lab's own machines, and the Pokémon is a Pokémon of the map ("Pokémon in the field"), so once it is set free the bay is simply empty.
  - *Cyrus's hall*: the grunts stand in ranks, a tile each, all facing the dais, and are there only for his speech.
- **A lift** (the Vista Lighthouse's, plan 02 · S13) runs in a shaft that is an inner wall like any other, cut away to the wainscot, and its car stands open in the shaft's south face: the tile is floor, a plate of ribbed steel `196,200,210` 24 texels wide between two dark jambs `66,68,84` four texels wide and as high as the wall is cut (28), with a sill of black and yellow stripes three texels deep along its open edge, as Iron Island's lift platforms have, and a call button on the east jamb, an amber dot `250,190,70` two texels across. Whoever takes the lift walks onto the plate, and the picture closes on them as on any way through. The car is a room of its own with nothing in it but the indicator on its back wall: a dark panel 24 by 12 with an arrow up and an arrow down ("Machines at work", below, has its lights and the ride). The lighthouse's gallery is a lab's room (pale tiles, grey-blue wainscot) with its corners cut off by inner walls, three windows side by side filling its back wall, and the field's own coin viewer before the middle one.
- **Machines at work** (plan 09 · L12; `SetPieces`). A machine that changes is a *held face*: every frame of it is painted the size of the face, and the room's sheet is redrawn in place only when the frame changes, as a fountain's is, never slid or tinted. What a machine shows is a function of the time since its script set it going, and the script waits for it.
  - *The healing machine's tray* (its top, 28 by 22 texels) is empty while nobody is being seen to: six sockets in two rows of three, each a dimple six texels across, a shade darker than the tray's floor (`104,112,138`) with a darker lip along its top. A Poké Ball is laid in a socket for each Pokémon of the team that is no Egg, from the back row's left, one every 0.35 s, each with a soft pop. When the last is down the heal's fanfare begins and the balls light for as long as it sounds (3.6 s): the tray's floor turns to pale light `206,236,250`, each ball's white half to `255,252,230` and its red to `255,150,130`, on the fanfare's own beat (116 a minute), lit for three quarters of each beat and plain for the last. Then the tray is empty again: the nurse has the balls back. Thirteen frames: empty, one ball to six, one to six lit. The console's screen under the tray (14 by 10 on its front) shows three lines of text at rest and, while the fanfare sounds, a white cross six texels wide and four tall on a brighter blue `150,206,250`, in the screen's upper half, since the counter before the machine hides the lower.
  - *A PC that is used* (a terminal: the one in a Pokémon Center's corner, the Battle Frontier's) has a screen that is dark until someone switches it on, where a computer nobody uses in the game keeps its lit one. Three frames of the monitor's front, 18 by 16: *dark*, the glass `36,40,58` with one paler line `58,64,88` slanting across its upper left and the lamp under it red `170,70,70`; *starting*, the deep blue `52,110,206` with a single bright line `190,230,252` across its middle and the lamp amber `250,190,70`; *on*, the screen as every lit one is (blue with three lines of text) and the lamp green `98,220,120`. Switched on, it shows the starting frame for 0.3 s and is on from then until it is switched off, when it shows the starting frame again for 0.2 s and goes dark. Each has its sound, and the script waits 0.4 s for the one and 0.3 s for the other.
  - *A lift's indicator* is dim at rest: both arrows in dull amber `132,100,46`. On a ride the arrow of the way it goes is lit `250,190,70` and runs: it is drawn at its place, then a texel further on its way, then two, a third of a hum each, and begins again; the other arrow stays dim. A ride is so many *hums* (the original's count: four, three in the Battle Tower's car) of half a second each, the car humming once to each, and half a second of arrival, with a two-note chime. Seven frames: at rest, three up, three down. The car shudders as it goes ("Life", "Set pieces").
  - *The Hall of Fame's machine* takes a ball in a socket for each of the team, an Egg's too, one every 0.35 s with a click, and then lights them to the same fanfare and on the same beat: a lit ball keeps its shape and takes a rim of light `255,246,196` in place of its dark outline, its white half `255,252,230`, its red `255,150,130`. The balls stay in their sockets, plain, until the room is left.
- **Spin arrows** (plan 19 · K7). On a floor that has a GBA spin's stop tiles (the Rocket Hideout's, the Viridian City Gym's), each tile that spins whoever steps on it carries an arrow painted on the floor the way it sends them: a chevron 16 texels wide and 12 deep in the middle of the tile, a pale amber `236,196,96` with a one-texel rim of dark brown `96,64,40` on its trailing edge, and a stop tile a ring 14 texels across in the same two colours with a dot at its middle. They are paint, flat on the floor, lit as the floor is: no glow after dark, nothing that stands up.
- **The Battle Pike's rooms** (plan 14 · B10; `BattlePike`, `BattlePikeRoom`) are a museum's rooms (`interior: Museum`), ours, thirteen tiles by eleven. The hall of three doors has its three ways on in the back wall at columns 3, 6 and 9, each painted as a doorway on the wall over its tile, the attendant who gives the hint standing at the west wall, a plant at each front corner and the way back to the annex at the middle of the front wall. A room behind a door has one way on in the back wall at its middle and none behind the player; a runner three tiles wide from the player's place to it, and whoever the room holds (a trainer or two, the attendant, the visitor, the Kirlia or the Dusclops of a strange power, the Pike Queen) stands off its middle column, so the way on is never closed. Nothing in either room tells which door hides what: only the attendant's words do.

### Gyms

A Gym (plan 01 · M9) is a room rebuilt to the original's plan ("Rooms", above) whose puzzle is part of the picture: what the puzzle moves is built apart from the room and drawn where the puzzle has it now (`GymPieces`, or a thing of the map drawn as a card), never baked into the floor.

- **The floor is ground, then hall.** A Gym's room (`interior: Gym`) bakes its floor as the field bakes ground (lawn, flowerbeds, paths and earth where its tiles say so), and where a tile is plain floor, wall or door (a wall's ground shows at the foot of an inner wall with no face to the south, a door's round the exit mat) it lays the hall's own tiles: 16 texels square in two close tones, a grout line at the right and the foot, a light edge at the left and the top. The tones are the Leader's, by the type the stage is themed on (`arenaType`): the dojo's pale boards `214,172,118` / `200,156,104`, the dark halls' slate `92,78,118` / `80,66,104`, the pool deck's white and pale blue `234,242,248` / `206,226,240`, a quarry's or a mine's stone `176,160,142` / `162,146,128`, the Snowpoint Gym's frosted pale blue `222,234,246` / `206,222,240`, the Sunyshore Gym's steel plate `150,160,182` / `138,148,170` (grout `92,100,124`), otherwise cream `236,232,214` / `222,216,196`.
- **The walls are the Leader's colours** on the room's wall strip: Eterna's greenhouse pale green `214,238,214` between white frames over green-stained boards `96,156,96`; Hearthome's violet `74,58,96` over plum `60,44,80` with a lilac rail; Veilstone's paper `240,226,196` over a wainscot of wood `150,98,60`; Pastoria's pale blue `214,234,248` over blue `70,130,200`; Canalave's steel sheeting `164,170,182` over a wainscot painted red `176,58,50`; Snowpoint's frost white `226,236,248` over a wainscot of glacier blue `92,142,204`; Sunyshore's night blue `54,64,104` with a motif and a rail of electric yellow `236,206,84` over a wainscot of steel `118,126,148`.
- **Tiers** (Oreburgh): a room whose map file gives heights is drawn with relief as the field is ("Relief", above): each tier a plate at its height, a step under three quarters of a tile a slope, anything more a face of rock (stone courses under a cap, the faces of the field's cliffs), stairs the field's stairs. The walls stand on the room's highest ground, and wherever the floor beside a wall is lower, the face of the ground runs from the floor up to the wall's foot, so a tier against a wall reads as cut into it. The kerb at the front stands on the front row's floor. A bridge between two tiers is the tier's own floor at the deck's height with a beam along each open side; the floor under it is in a flat dark shade `52,46,52`, never the field's green one.
- **The water and its buttons** (Pastoria): the water is a sheet over the whole pool at the floor's level, `64,136,214` with rows of small waves (a crest `132,194,244` over a trough `44,104,184`), drawn a little under the floor so the rafts and walkways stand out of it. What can't be walked is never drawn as floor: a blocked tile of the pool (the strips between the walkways, and between them and the walk round the room, which the original's plates set at the walkways' height) is drawn at the pool's floor with its water always over it, and the pool's floor itself is dry only while the water is all the way down, when it is walked. Each tile of the floating floor that someone stands on is a raft: a block of planks `178,132,84` (light `210,168,112`, seams and nails `122,86,56`) that rides on the water. A button is a pale rim `176,184,196` with a raised cap of its colour (blue `70,120,222`, green `74,176,92`, orange `236,138,52`) and a lighter disc on its top; the button pressed last is sunk to half its height.
- **The flower clock** (Eterna): the face is a disc of foliage eleven tiles across laid on the ground, set thick with flowers in a ring of each colour from the rim in, scalloped where the rings meet, with a pale band at the rim; the twelve hours are stones round it and the pin a stone boss. The hands are walks of wooden boards edged with leaves, two texels above the face: the minute hand 22 texels wide, reaching the rim; the hour hand 26 wide, a tile shorter, its point raised to 9 texels where it is hopped over. They turn on the pin at the original's pace, two minutes of the clock a frame at thirty frames a second (an hour a second), the camera going to the face first and to the fountain that drains. A fountain is a pool in a stone kerb whose water falls by its own depth over two seconds.
- **Punching bags and stacks of tyres** (Veilstone) stand in the way like people and are drawn as cards, as the field's obstacles are: a bag of red leather `206,62,58` banded in white at top and foot and hanging from a chain, 24 by 44 texels; three tyres `58,58,72` stacked, 30 by 30. A kicked bag draws back half a tile for a second, then runs along its track at the original's speed (3.75 tiles a second) with the camera after it when it runs out of the picture; one that can't go only swings. A stack it runs into topples and is gone.
- **The dark rooms** (Hearthome) are a black fog over the whole picture, the dark of "Caves" (`8,6,12`) but never quite opaque: the original's 109 of 127 in the first room (86%), 119 in the second (94%), 91 in either once the Relic Badge is won (72%). It lifts round the player and round every trainer, each a light of their own: clear for 1.5 tiles, soft over 1.1 more, the circles lying on the ground and joining where they meet. Flash does nothing there.
- **The doors** (Hearthome) are arched doors of dark wood in a gilt frame, 28 by 50 texels, on the back wall at each way on, each with its sign in gilt over the middle: a circle, a square, a triangle, a sun, a ring, a moon, a star, a heart. The sign of the door that leads on stands a little above the floor where the original puts its clue, gilt on a dark disc 22 texels across, and like everything else it is seen only when a light comes near it. A pad between the entrance and Fantina's room is a ring of pale violet light on the floor. A bollard is dark polished stone `84,70,108` with a gilt cap, 16 by 28.
- **The shaft and its lifts** (Canalave): the room's floor is the bottom of a mine shaft, the hall's quarry stone where the lowest floor can be walked and nothing drawn where it can't (the dark of the room shows through, `20,18,24`). Each floor above is ten tiles over the last, a deck of steel grating over every tile its map lets one walk: plates `150,156,166` with a grid of slots `96,100,112` every four texels and a light edge `196,202,212` at the top and left, six texels thick, with its edge faces shown wherever the deck ends. A floor shows only once the viewer has risen a tile toward it, as the original's props do: from the ground the shaft is open to the dark above. A lift between floors (and the two shafts from the bottom to the top) is a red plate `196,58,50` with a band of yellow `236,196,60` and black hazard stripes at its edge; a cart that runs along a floor is a yellow plate with two dark arrows pointing the ways it runs, on a pair of dark rails `62,64,74` along its whole track. A platform's top stands two texels over its floor, six deep, and it moves with the player on it at the original's speed (fifteen tiles a second); where it has gone from a floor, its place there is a hole.
- **The ice and its snowballs** (Snowpoint): the floor is the field's ice ("Ground": `150,200,236`, its glints) laid over the original's bowl as relief, ring below ring, and it is the ground under everything there, as a cave's floor is under its rock: no lawn shows where rounded corners meet. Each slope of ice is streaked down its fall, five light lines `206,232,250` a texel wide and six to twelve long, each with a line of blue shade `124,176,222` beside it, so a ramp reads as a ramp. Where the original's ground stops a slide (a patch here and there, the steps down the middle of each slope) it is the field's snow, and Candice's dais and the way in are the hall's tiles. A snowball stands in the way like a person and is drawn as a card 30 by 30 of the scenery, not a sprite: lit as the ice it stands on, it comes out about as bright as the snow beside it, where a sprite's warmer light greyed it to a rock. It is a smooth ball of packed snow lit from the upper left (`204,230,255`, then `186,210,244`), shaded blue toward the lower right (`148,182,232`) and deepest where it sits on the ice (`110,148,218`), with a few sparkles on its lit side and a soft blue outline, below white. A slide with speed bursts it ("Life": obstacles giving way) and goes on through where it stood. The slide keeps a run's pace on flat ice and quickens as it gathers speed down the slopes, by the original's frames a tile (four, three, then two); a slip back down a slope goes at a quarter of the run's pace, the player still facing up it and not walking.
- **The gears and their walkways** (Sunyshore): the room is a power station's floor over the dark. Its floors are the hall's steel plate laid as relief at the original's heights (one, four and seven tiles), and they stand on faces of their own: a pale steel edge `206,212,226`, a band of hazard stripes in yellow `236,196,60` and black `40,40,48`, then panels of dark steel `74,82,104` with rivets `140,148,168`, sixteen rows to a panel. Under the gears there is nothing (the dark of the room, `20,18,24`), the floors' faces running down into it to two tiles under the room's lowest floor, the same depth wherever a walkway meets a floor, so every floor reads as a deck. A flat gear is a toothed wheel lying on its hub, 86 texels across, gold `228,184,58` (light `250,220,118`, shade `164,122,36`) or, for the original's "alt" gears, steel blue `110,150,214` (light `164,198,244`, shade `66,96,160`), with a dark steel boss `60,66,86` on its hub; its arms are walkways 26 texels wide reaching two tiles past the hub, decks of pale steel `186,194,210` in plates eight texels long with a yellow line along each side, six texels thick, their tops level with the floor. A gear on edge is a bar of the same deck five tiles long and a tile thick, its two ends in hazard stripes, turning on a dark axle with a toothed drum of the gear's colour at each end; it is a bridge while it lies flat and a striped post standing up out of the dark while it doesn't. All of a room's gears turn together at the original's pace (5.625 degrees a frame at thirty frames a second: a quarter turn in about half a second), each its own way, the field waiting. A button on a hub is a steel rim `176,184,196` with a cap of yellow `248,214,72` and a black arrow round it showing the way the gear under it turns (counter-clockwise, clockwise, or two arrows for a half turn), standing two texels over the hub and never turning with it. A doorway on to the next room is a steel door `120,128,150` in a yellow frame on the back wall.
- **The slabs on the lava** (Blackthorn, plan 20 · J11, part c): each slab is a raft of basalt `70,62,66` (lit edge `98,88,90`, cracks `46,40,44`) lying two texels over the room's floor, five tiles by seven (the middle one four by eight), turned and slid as the puzzle has it now (`BlackthornPlatforms`, drawn by `GymPieces`). Its floor of three tiles by six is paved in pale stone `150,140,128` / `138,128,118` with grout `104,96,90`; its rim is the bare basalt. On the floor's second row stand its three buttons, discs of red-orange `220,92,48` (light `248,150,96`, dark `150,52,30`) in a dark ring: the middle one with a ring of an arrow round it (the turn), the two either side with an arrow pointing outward along the slab's length (the way it slides). Its sides are basalt eight texels deep with a glowing seam of lava `236,120,40` along the foot. The lava under it is the room's own floor; nothing of the slab is baked into it.
- **The Mossdeep Gym's arrows** (plan 21 · H8): the room is the original's islands of floor joined by its warp pads. Each arrow tile is a square plate of its colour lying a hair over the floor, 28 texels with a bevel (lit top and left edge, dark bottom and right), and on it a pale arrow `236,232,244`, a head over the near half and a shaft six texels wide, pointing the way the tile carries whoever stands on it; four make a loop whose arrows run round it anticlockwise as seen from above. The colours: yellow `226,186,60`, blue `72,124,206`, green `78,160,92`, purple `144,92,184`, red `200,70,62`, each with a light and a shade a step either side. A floor switch is a round plate of its colour 24 texels across, its rim darker and a lit boss in its middle. A statue is a card 22 by 38 of the scenery: a small figure of pale lilac stone `186,176,208` (light `214,206,230`, shade `132,120,160`), its hands joined before its chest, on a squared plinth a step darker `150,140,176`; nothing of it is white. Stepped on, a switch moves the people and statues on its colour's arrows a step round their loop at a walk's pace, keeping their facing, each turned a quarter anticlockwise as it sets off; the arrows themselves never move.
- **The Snowbelle Gym's turntables** (plan 23, Kalos's Gyms): Wulfric's hall is two platforms of the Ice hall's floor
  with the dark between them, and over the dark five turntables of ice. A hub is a slab of pale ice `176,214,236` a tile
  square and six texels thick, its top level with the floor, a ring of deep blue `96,132,176` 18 texels across two texels
  proud of it, and a boss of the pale ice in the ring; an arm is a slab of the same ice five texels thick, three texels in
  from the tile's sides, a tile long out from the hub's edge, with a rim of the deep blue across its far end. An arm is
  built lying north and turned a quarter clockwise for each way round; it is drawn where it lies now, with no turning
  between (the step onto the hub is the turn).
- **The Santalune Gym's web** (plan 23, Kalos's Gyms): Viola's hall is the Bug hall's floor at the door and under her,
  and between them the dark with a web of silk strung over it. A ring (a strand that always holds) is a band of pale silk
  `226,228,236` (light `244,246,250`, shade `176,180,196`) eight texels wide and three thick, running east and west
  through the middle of its tile, its top level with the floor; a knot is a pad of the same silk twenty texels square, a
  texel proud of the ring, with a boss of amber `214,168,82` in its middle. A radial strand (north and south) is the same
  silk with three drops of dew on it while the dew lies there, beads of clear blue `120,186,232` (light `196,230,250`,
  shade `70,128,184`) six texels square and three high; once the dew has run off it is a thread of dull grey silk
  `150,150,162` four texels wide sagging two texels under the floor's height. The dew moves all at once as a knot is
  stepped on: there is no running between.
- **The Cyllage Gym's wall** (plan 23, Kalos's Gyms): Grant's climbing wall lies over the Rock hall's floor in two pitches
  with a ledge of the floor between. Each tile of a pitch is a slab of dark rock `118,104,96` (light `146,130,118`, shade
  `84,74,70`) lying a hair over the floor, cracked with lines of the shade; a grip is a knob of terracotta `206,108,62`
  (light `236,150,98`, shade `150,72,42`) twelve texels square and four high in the tile's middle, the only place a climber
  stands. A rope is a cord of hemp `206,178,120` (shade `150,124,80`) six texels wide down its tile's middle with a knot of
  the shade every eight texels. The pitches are flat on the floor: the camera's lean makes them a wall.
- **The Shalour Gym's rink** (plan 23, Kalos's Gyms): Korrina's hall is a rink over a pit. The floor at the door and under
  her and the rails between are the Fighting hall's boards; on a rail's tile beside a gap stands a ramp, a wedge of steel
  `150,160,182` (light `196,204,220`, shade `100,108,128`) twenty-four texels wide in two steps (four and seven texels
  high) over the half of the tile on the side it faces, with a stripe of hazard yellow `236,196,60` at its lip; the ramp
  faces the way the gap is hopped now, and turns round, all ramps at once, when a lever is stepped on. A lever is a post
  of the steel eight texels square and ten high with a handle of red `206,62,58` leaning west or east by the points.
- **The Coumarine Gym's tree** (plan 23, Kalos's Gyms): Ramos's hall is a vine tree in three tiers of the Grass hall's
  floor, two and four tiles up, with the field's faces between. A climbing pole is a vine of green `86,150,74` (light
  `126,186,98`, shade `56,104,52`) six texels across up the face from the tier below to the tier above, with leaves of the
  light green; a sliding pole is a pole of smooth wood `176,128,82` (light `210,166,112`, shade `122,86,56`) the same size.
  The hanging pole is the vine, and hangs a third of the face's height from the tier above until its switch is pulled,
  then reaches the tier below. The switch is a loop of vine `86,150,74` round a flower of pink `226,120,168` lying on its
  tile. A hedge of the room's plants parts the top tier.
- **The Sootopolis Gym's ice** (plan 21 · H8, part c): the room's floor of thin ice is the room's own ice; what the puzzle adds lies a hair over it, a tile at a time. A tile stepped on carries a crack, 32 texels square over nothing: five runs of dark blue `74,108,140` from a broken middle out toward the edges, each texel with a pale rim `232,246,252` on its lit side (up and left), and a star of chips where they meet. A flight of stairs above a field not yet crossed is glazed over, each step covered by a sheet of pale blue ice `176,214,236` lit along its top edge `222,242,252` and darker at its foot `132,176,210`, with two short glints; the glaze goes from a flight, all at once, as the last tile below it is cracked, and the stairs under it are the room's own. Nothing falls: a tile that gives way takes the player down behind the fade.
- **Don't**: bake a puzzle's moving part into the floor, light a dark room fully, or let a bag or a hand move by itself while the player can act: the field waits until each has stopped.

### The hub's halls

Plan 41 · EG2, EG4, EG7: rooms drawn by hand behind the hub's doors (`Data/maps/Hub_*.json`), our own throughout.

- **The Laurel Gallery** (`Hub_HallOfChampions`, interior `Museum`): the hall of Champions, 19 by 13, a rug down its middle
  aisle, a painting each side of a wall emblem, the registrar's counter at the east. Its **plinths** (`PropType.Plinth`,
  `HubArt`, a tile each, blocking by the room's own tiles) stand one for each region finished, in the chain's order on two
  columns each side of the aisle, with EARLIER TEAMS at the east and the circuit's at the head of the aisle: a block of
  pale stone `226,222,212` (light `244,242,236`, shade `190,184,172`) 22 texels square and 16 high, a slab of the region's
  laurel colour over it 24 wide and 3 high, a wreath of that colour carved on its face and a wreath 16 texels across
  standing on the slab, its leaves in the colour's light and dark by turns, open at its top and tied with a gold ribbon
  `236,206,96`. **The laurel colours** (one table, `HubArt.LaurelColour`, which the Hall of Fame's tabs take too): Sinnoh
  `132,120,196`, Kanto `206,86,72`, Johto `212,168,64`, Hoenn `66,160,112`, Unova `96,104,124`, Kalos `70,128,212`, Alola
  `236,140,64`, Galar `186,60,96`, Paldea `164,84,168`; EARLIER TEAMS bronze `170,120,74`, the circuit gold `232,196,84`.
- **The Laurel Court** (`Hub_Arena`): the circuit's arena on the Champion's room's look (`hall` `Champion`: marble, ivory and
  gold), 15 by 16, four statues on plinths along the carpet, the herald before the back wall and the finished regions'
  Champions in a row along it while a final waits.
- **The Open Door Agency** (`Hub_Office`, interior `House`): Looker's office, a cluttered room of boxes and maps (the
  outline sheet's 6b), 13 by 10. A desk across the back with Looker behind it half-disguised as a clerk (`looker_clerk`: a
  blue visor and waistcoat, spectacles), his Croagunk asleep on a cushion beside it, crates stacked in the corners (things of
  the map, `Crate`), rolled maps on the tables (`TownMap`), a bookshelf, a pile of books and a blackboard of pinned notes.

### The League's rooms

The rooms behind the League's door (plan 01 · M9, part 2d) are halls as the Gyms are ("Gyms", above: the original's plan, a floor baked from its tiles, hall tiles where they are plain floor, what moves drawn apart by `GymPieces`), in one family: gold on every door, lift and rail, and a look to each room.

- **The four rooms** take theirs from the type of their master (`arenaType`), each a place more than a hall. *Aaron's garden*: lawn with flowers and a path of earth from door to door, a hedge and trees in tubs where the original's room is closed; hall tiles of mossy stone `150,176,120` / `138,164,110`; walls leaf green `196,222,170` over a wainscot of bark `126,98,64`. *Bertha's canyon*: a floor of sand between shelves of rock two tiles high, drawn as the field's relief with its rock faces, boulders on the shelves and along the front row, where the shelves stop so the front wall's kerb stands on the floor; tiles of sandstone `214,186,140` / `202,172,126`; walls clay `222,196,150` over terracotta `170,110,70`. *Flint's causeway*: a floor of basalt `92,74,78` / `80,64,70` seven tiles wide over a bed of lava a tile below (the room's tiles there are void, as under the Sunyshore Gym's gears); walls deep red `122,50,46` with a motif and a rail of brass `226,150,70` over charcoal `70,58,64`. *Lucian's study*: tiles of violet `170,140,206` / `156,126,194` with four columns; walls lilac `206,186,232` over plum `110,76,150`.
- **The rest have no type** and take a look of their own (`hall` in the map file). *The League's own stone* (the passages): tiles of slate blue `128,140,172` / `116,128,160`; walls navy `58,70,112` with a motif and a rail of gold `226,190,96` over a wainscot `84,96,138`. *The Champion's room*: tiles of marble `228,226,216` / `212,210,200`, never whiter, so the daylight doesn't bloom them blank; walls ivory `236,230,212` with gold `222,190,104` over a wainscot of pale gold `206,178,110`. *The Hall of Fame* and the hallway to it: tiles of dark polished stone `58,60,84` / `50,52,76` that mirror whoever stands on them, as a puddle does ("Puddles"); walls midnight `40,44,74` with gold `214,180,92` over deep violet `70,60,96`.
- **The faces the floors stand on** (a shaft's wall, the causeway's sides, the wall under the Champion's gallery) are courses of dressed stone twelve rows high, their joints half a block along from course to course, each block lit along its top and left and shaded at its foot and right, under a cap with a pale edge and a band of gold `226,190,96`: slate `96,104,134` in the passages, basalt `70,56,62` over the lava, sandstone `196,160,112` under the walls of Bertha's room, marble `196,190,174` in the Champion's room.
- **Lava** is a sheet over the whole bed, 0.45 of a tile under the causeway: molten orange `226,96,36` with pools of a deeper red `200,70,30`, veins of `250,170,60` that brighten and fade and a few sparks of `255,214,110`, and plates of dark crust `120,40,30` (edge `84,28,26`), each an irregular slab lit along its upper edge by the glow under it, that rock a texel or two to and fro: four frames at three a second, in a pattern two tiles across so no plate is seen twice in a row. Nothing of it is white: the glow is the bloom's.
- **Doors.** A way on is a door in the back wall, 30 by 56 texels in a frame of gold `226,190,96` (light `250,226,150`, shade `170,130,60`) with a round head: open, the dark of the room beyond `20,18,28` with two steps of shade at its jambs; shut (a room of the Four until its master is beaten), two leaves of dark bronze `70,78,116` in sunken panels, studded with gold, with a bar across them. The door behind the challenger is in the front wall, which is cut away to its kerb like every room's: shut, it is a gate of the same bronze as high as two kerbs, capped in gold, across the gap it closed; it comes down with the original's clank and is not there before. The door from the Pokémon Center is in a block of the room's own wall that stands two tiles out from the back wall at its full height, a pilaster of gold at each side and the door between them.
- **The lifts** are a platform three tiles by two: a slab eight texels thick whose top stands two over its floor, bronze plates `92,100,140` in a rim of gold with a gold ring and a ball's line at its middle, hazard chevrons of gold along its north and south edges. It rises at the original's pace (3.75 tiles a second) with the player on it, the field waiting, along a shaft whose wall is the face above; a pair of gold rails runs up that wall from the platform's corners to the floor above.
- **Carpets** lie on the floor from a way in to where it leads, a texel above it: deep blue `48,70,150` edged with gold in the Champion's room, crimson `170,44,56` edged with gold in the hallway and the Hall of Fame, a border three texels in and a small diamond every tile.
- **The Champion's piers** are four blocks of marble two tiles by three and 76 texels high, panelled, with a band of gold at head and foot. **The Hall of Fame's machine** stands against the back wall: a console of dark steel `60,66,96` three tiles wide and 20 texels high edged in gold, six sockets for Poké Balls in a ring on its top, and behind it a screen 60 by 44 in a gold frame, lit blue `96,170,236` with a ball's outline. As the Champion's team is entered (plan 02 · S14) a Poké Ball stands in each socket for each of the team, laid one after another: the field's own ball (red `218,62,58` over white `240,240,236`, a band and a button of `58,52,72`, outlined), ten texels across on a card of twelve, upright on the console's top. Statues on plinths line the hallway and the Hall where the original's pillars stand.
- **Don't**: light a room of the League white, or let a door move while the player can act: the door shuts and opens inside its script, and the lift holds the field until it has stopped.

### Characters

- **Sculpted with the SDF kit** (G6): each character is one smooth body of signed-distance shapes (`SdfModel`: ellipsoids, round cones, round boxes, tori, cylinders) joined with smooth unions, carved with cuts and coloured with paint, meshed by surface nets into a skinned mesh (`SdfMesher`, cells of 1/112 unit), with the clothes a second one over it ("Layered clothes", below) and cached on disk (`SdfCache`). The same model is the 3D trainer in battle and the source of the field sprites (`CharacterModels`).
- **Proportions**: chibi. Children (the player, the rival, youngsters, lasses) are about 1.2 units tall with a head of radius 0.27, nearly half their height; adults have a longer body and a slightly smaller head (radius 0.25), little children (plan 11 · C2) a shorter one, then `CharacterStyle.Height` sizes each type. The player reads at about 130 px tall on screen.
- **From Kalos on, not chibi** (plan 23 · Z1, the user's ruling of 2026-11-02): the people of Kalos and the regions after it have realistic proportions, a head about a seventh of the height, a long body and legs, and walk freely (plan 30's free movement). Until plan 30 rebuilds the kit for it, Kalos's people are drawn with this kit at the least chibi it can be: every one of its own cast (the neighbours, the friends, the professor) on the `Adult` build, which has the longer body and the smaller head, at a `height` of 1.0 or more, so none of them is a child's chibi figure; the player keeps the player's look until plan 30. What a figure that isn't chibi needs, and this kit has not: a head of radius about 0.13 on a 1.7-unit body, so a separate neck and shoulders; arms and legs of two bones each that bend at the elbow and knee (the 19-bone skeleton's limbs are single sticks under the chibi shells); hands with fingers enough to hold a ball; a face that reads at a seventh of the height, so eyes, brows and mouth of their own geometry rather than a decal patch; field sprites baked at a scale where the figure is about 2.4 tiles tall, so the field camera's lens and every sprite card's size change with them; eight facings, or four facings turned freely, for plan 30's movement. None of it is built in Z1. Plan 30 · FM4 builds that kit beside this one ("Grown characters", below), and Kalos's people move to it with FM10.
- **Parts**: mitten hands with a thumb; shoes with a sole in their own colour (white under red or blue shoes); sleeves ending in a cuff, or short sleeves at two thirds of the upper arm; a collar or a scarf; stripes painted across the shirt (light on a coloured shirt, the accent colour on a white one); skirts and coat tails on a bone of their own so they swing; the player's backpack with straps and a buckle; a beard (`CharacterStyle.Beard`, plan 11 · C6) in the hair's colour, a full mass round the chin and jaw below the mouth with a sideburn up to each ear, never over the mouth or the cheeks, so the face's patch keeps the eyes and the mouth; glasses (`CharacterStyle.Glasses`, the frame's colour) are no shape but part of the face, drawn by both faces below. Colour edges are painted crisp (waistline, sleeves, soles), never blended.
- **Layered clothes** (every look): what is worn over the body is a mesh of its own (`CharacterRig.Clothes`, with its own outline shell, meshed in cells of 0.8 of the body's), sculpted and meshed apart from the body and skinned to the same skeleton, so it never melts into what is under it. The body under a garment is slimmed (an arm to 0.85 of its radius where a sleeve covers it, the chest to 0.92 of its width) so nothing pokes through; a garment stands 0.012 units off it, about a cell and a half. A garment ends in a flat hem cut straight across (never a rounded end), the cut face and a strip a cell wide above it a shade darker (0.62), which with its own outline shell gives an ink line at every hem: a shirt's or a jacket's just below the hips over the trousers (higher, at their waist, under overalls), a coat's below the knees, a robe's at the ankles, a skirt's, a sleeve's at the wrist with the hand coming out of it or at two thirds of the upper arm. A **jacket** (`CharacterStyle.Jacket`, the player's) has a ribbed band a shade darker (0.86) above its hem and at each cuff, a zip line down its front with a metal pull and two pocket flaps; a **coat** stands open over the shirt, the accent colour showing between its fronts to the waist and a line where they meet below it, with two pocket flaps low on its tails, unless it is **buttoned** (`Buttoned`, the girl's): a placket line from the collar to the hem and three buttons in the accent colour; a **robe** is closed with its sash. What is worn over the shirt lies on the clothes' layer: overalls' bib painted on it and their straps following its surface over the shoulders; an apron's bib painted on it, its panel a piece of its own along the front of whatever is under it from the waist to the knees, tied at the waist; a vest painted over the shirt's body with the shirt down its open front. A collar is the accent colour's, or the garment's own under a scarf, which is wrapped over it with its tail hanging in front; a backpack's straps follow the clothes' surface round the shoulders, a buckle at each front end. A **swimsuit** is the body's own, painted on it, with nothing over it. The trousers, the shoes, the hands, the head and what is held stay the body's, the trousers ending in a cuff over the shoe's top with no blend.
- **Hair is sculpted in locks**: tapering tubes laid along the skull from the crown (from the hairline, for swept hair) to pointed tips, over a snug cap, with small blends so grooves show between them; bangs end just above the eyes; long hair falls over a smooth mass behind and swings; spiky hair stands in tufts. Under a beret or cap the locks start below its edge, so no hair shows through the hat, and they lie close enough at the back that the hat's band doesn't show between them. A hat sits on the hair, never in it: the nurse's cap stands on top of the hair, and a cap's peak comes out of the crown's front edge with the bangs below it. White hair is a shaded grey-white (`190,194,210`), never pure white. A bow (`Headwear.Bow`) sits on the hair at the top of the back of the head: two loops 0.8 of the head's radius across, tilted up and out, round a knot, with two short tails falling behind, in its colour with the knot in its band's. A wrestler's mask (`Headwear.Mask`) is a hood over the crown, the sides and the back down to the jaw, the face left bare, with a crest in its band's colour from the brow to the nape; no lock of hair grows under it. A fedora (`Headwear.Fedora`) is a cap's crown taller and rounder, a brim all round at the crown's foot (1.45 of the head's radius across the sides, 1.35 to the front, so the eyes still show from the field's camera) and a band in its band's colour just above the brim; the locks start below its edge as under a cap. Four cuts came with the second half's cast (plan 11 · C6): an afro (`HairCut.Afro`), a round mass of hair over the crown, the back and the sides, its front stopping at the brow, with a crown of small round curls on its surface, no bangs and never a lock; a bun (`HairCut.Bun`), the swept cut's locks combed back from the hairline into a round knot high on the back of the head; braids (`HairCut.Braids`), the short cut with its bangs and two plaits from under the ears down over the front of the shoulders to the chest, beads one after another, each tied at its end in the band's colour (`hatBand`, which a look without a hat may set) with a short tuft below; bunches (`HairCut.Bunches`), the short cut with its bangs and two thick tails tied high at the sides of the head, standing out and curling down to the jaw and swinging with the hair, tied in the band's colour. Every cut stays inside the sprite's frame: no wider than 0.82 of a unit and no higher than 1.5.
- **The named cast** (plan 11 · C5, C6): someone the story names is a look of their own under the original's object name (`roark`, `mira`, `crasher_wake`), or under their overlay's type where the original has no object for them (`bebe`, `poketch_president`), and is known at a glance by hair, hat and colours, never by a face alone: Cheryl's long chestnut hair and a long green skirt under a peach blouse; Mira, a small girl with long pale-gold hair under a big orange bow and a pink dress; Bebe's short dark hair and an orange cardigan over a cream top; Crasher Wake, the tallest, in a blue mask with a yellow crest, bare-chested over blue trousers and yellow boots; the Pokétch Company's president, a man with swept grey hair and a moustache in a navy suit with a red tie. The second half's (C6): Commander Saturn's spiky steel-blue hair over the team's pale uniform and dark trousers; Riley in a navy fedora and a long navy coat over a blue shirt; Byron, big and bearded, spiky brown hair and a steel-blue work shirt over brown trousers; Candice's dark braids tied in white over a green sweater and a navy skirt; Volkner's spiky pale-gold hair and a navy jacket with a yellow collar; Jasmine's brown bunches tied in orange and a white dress with a teal collar; Marley, small and pale, short black hair and a dark violet dress; Buck, tanned, spiky brown hair, a black vest with an orange collar and grey trousers; Aaron's spiky teal hair and a black-green coat with a yellow collar, a bug's colours; Bertha, short, silver hair in a bun and an ochre cardigan over a brown skirt; Flint's red afro over a black vest with an orange collar; Lucian's long purple hair, glasses and a burgundy coat; Palmer, the tallest of them, his son's spiky gold hair and orange over a green coat and cream trousers; Teala, the Pokémon Centers' attendant upstairs and the Battle Tower's, brown hair in a bun and a teal jacket and skirt; Professor Oak's spiky grey hair and a white lab coat over a red shirt and khaki trousers; Thorton, short, mauve-grey hair, glasses and a pale grey coat with an orange collar; Argenta's silver-lavender bun and a long purple coat with gold; Darach's swept auburn hair and a butler's black tailcoat with a white cravat; Dahlia's dark afro and a red dress with gold; Lady Caitlin's long pale-gold hair and a long pink gown. Kanto's first (plan 19 · K3): the rival's spiky chestnut hair and a purple shirt over dark trousers (in battle too: the rival's class stands as `rival` or `blue`, by whose rival he is); Brock, tall, spiky dark brown hair, a green shirt under an orange-brown vest. Plan 19 · K5's: Lt. Surge, the tallest, spiky pale-gold hair and an olive soldier's vest over a khaki shirt; the S.S. Anne's captain in a white cap with a navy band and a white coat, a moustache; Mr. Fuji, small, short white hair and a long plum robe; a Channeler (her class in battle too), long black hair and a long white robe with a purple sash. Plan 19 · K5, part b's: Erika, dark hair in a bun under a red bow and a long green robe over a yellow sash; Sabrina, long straight dark-green hair and a long red top over white trousers; Giovanni, tall, slicked dark hair and a long orange-brown suit coat over a black shirt (the class Boss stands as him); a Biker (the Biker's and the Cue Ball's class in battle too), a spiky dark crop and a black jacket with a red band over dark jeans; a cook, a white cap and a white jacket over dark trousers. Plan 19 · K6's: Koga, a ninja's dark purple hood and mask over a dark grey suit with a deep red sash; a Juggler (his class in battle too), spiky orange hair and a striped red and yellow top over purple trousers; a Bird Keeper (his class in battle too), a brown cap and a sky-blue vest over khaki trousers.
