Plan 19 · K6, part b's: Blaine, short, white tufts of hair and a white moustache, dark round glasses and a long white coat with a flame-red collar; a Burglar (his class in battle too), a dark cap and a striped black and white jumper over dark trousers; and the classes K6a left in the plain default, as they stand in battle: a Tamer, a broad-brimmed brown hat and a khaki shirt with a red neckerchief, a moustache; one of the Twins (Sinnoh's Twins too), dark bunches and a pink dress; one of the Crush Kin, a dark bun and an orange training top over black shorts. Plan 19 · K7's: Lorelei, long wavy dark-red hair, glasses and a long violet dress; Bruno, tall and broad, a spiky dark crop, bushy brows and a short-sleeved black top over pale grey training trousers; Agatha, small and old, grey hair in a bun and a long plum dress with a pale shawl; Lance, spiky red hair and a long black coat lined in red over a navy suit; Celio, short brown hair and a sky-blue shirt over grey trousers; a Cooltrainer (his class in battle too), a shock of dark blue hair and a royal blue jacket over black trousers, and the woman's (hers too), long red hair and a royal blue top and skirt; a Swimmer, cropped dark hair and a navy swimsuit, and the woman's, a white swimming cap and a red swimsuit (the classes in battle too). Plan 20 · J14's (Johto's): Bugsy, a boy with a pale-green bowl cut in a green tunic; Chuck, the biggest, bald and bushy-browed, in a cream gi top and blue trousers; Pryce, small and old, white hair and beard, a blue coat and a white scarf; Clair, a dark-blue ponytail, a royal-blue cape and a gold-trimmed skirt; Will, swept violet hair and a long pale coat with a violet collar; Karen, long black hair and a black dress with a red collar; Kurt, old, white hair under a red-banded headband and a brown apron over a tan shirt; Eusine, long blue hair and a long white coat with a violet scarf; the friend (Lyra), a chestnut ponytail under a wide white hat with a pink band, a pink top and blue shorts; Mr. Pokémon, white hair, glasses and a moustache in an orange coat; Red, a red cap with a white band and a red top, a yellow pack; Team Rocket's four executives in white under the red mark (Proton's short grey hair, Petrel's spiky lilac hair, Ariana's red ponytail and white skirt, Archer's swept dark hair and moustache) and the woman grunt (a black cap, a ponytail, a black skirt); and the classes new to Johto: a Firebreather in a gold headband and a red shirt, a Poké Maniac in a wide brown hat, glasses and a red pack, a Boarder in a blue beanie and yellow goggles over an orange jacket, a Teacher with her hair up, glasses and a cream blouse, and the Sage, a long grey-violet robe. Plan 20 · J14, second part: the Medium (hers in battle too), long black hair and a deep violet robe with a pale scarf; the five Kimono Girls of the Dance Theater, each in the colour of the Eevee she dances with and each with her hair done otherwise (Zuki, a black bun and indigo with gold; Naoko, long plum hair and lavender; Miki, an auburn ponytail and flame orange; Sayo, blue-black bunches and sky blue; Kuni, golden braids and yellow; the class stands as the pink Kimono Girl, and as the person's own of the five when they are the one in the battle); Elder Li, small, bald and white-bearded in a grey-blue robe, and the Dragon's Den's elder in a deep red-brown robe with gold; the Radio Tower's Director, short grey hair, glasses and a moustache in a wine-red coat; and the Pokéathlon Dome's owner, a blue visor and a blue and white sports top. The people HeartGold names by a class's object stand as that class (a hiker, a fisherman, a Poké Maniac, a Firebreather, a black belt, a camper, a picnicker, a worker, a sailor, a juggler, a swimmer, a Psychic, and the Gym Guide for the sunglasses of its Gyms), and Bill, Crasher Wake of Route 47 and the Mediums as themselves. A Leader, a member of the Elite Four, a Commander and a Frontier Brain stand in battle as their own look.
- **The salon's parts** (plan 43 · LF3; `clothes.json`'s `hair`, `hairColours`, `eyeColours`, `skinTones`, `dyes`): every cut both kits draw (the twelve of `HairCut`); the hair's eight colours (black 42 36 48, brown 90 60 44, chestnut 138 78 44, blond 224 188 108, copper 196 98 46, ash 168 164 172, plum 110 60 120, teal 46 124 128); the eyes' six (dark, brown, hazel, green, blue, grey); and the six skin tones plan 11 designed and never wrote (fair 253 230 210, light 244 208 176, warm 226 174 134, tan 200 142 98, brown 154 100 66, deep 110 69 46). An outfit names them only once one is chosen (`PLAYER@-.-.-.-.-.Bun,copper,-,-`), so every look before them keeps its name, sprites and meshes. **The dyes** are sixteen of ours, each one main colour and an accent laid over a garment's own two, the garment's shape kept: cherry, coral, peach, sunflower, lemon, leaf, mint, teal (a café's gift, sold at no counter), sky, navy, violet, plum, rose, cream, charcoal and snow.
- **The first trainers' parts** (plan 11 · C2): four cuts, five hats and a build. A **bowl** (`HairCut.Bowl`) is one round mass a little fuller than the skull, cut by a plane straight across the brow (its edge just above the brows, where the eyes stay clear from the field's camera) that slopes down past the tops of the ears to the nape, with no locks or bangs over it; Team Galactic's grunts wear it. A **ponytail** is the short cut's locks with a tail tied high at the back in the band's colour, falling to the shoulders on the hair's bone so it swings (under a hat the tie is lower, below the hat's edge). **Curls** are small round curls a fifth of the head's radius over the cap, a row of them at the fringe whose lowest stands clear of the eyes, none over the face. **Bald** is no cap: a band of short locks round the back of the head from ear to ear, the crown bare skin. A **helmet** (`Headwear.Helmet`) is a smooth shell over the crown whose front edge stands above the brows, a stripe in the band's colour from front to back over its top and a short peak over the eyes; a **hard hat** a round crown with a ridge in the band's colour from front to back and a brim all round its foot, wider at the front; a **beanie** a snug knitted crown with a rolled cuff in the band's colour and a pompom on top; a **bandana** a cloth over the crown down to the brow, knotted at the back with two short tails, in the band's colour where it is tied; a **headband** a band round the brow over the bangs, with two tails at the back that hang, the hair showing above it as it is. Under a helmet, a hard hat, a beanie or a bandana the hair grows from below its edge, as under a cap. The **little** build (`BodyBuild.Little`) is a smaller child (the twins, the boy with his game): a child's head on a body four fifths as tall and a little narrower, about one unit tall. Team Galactic's grunts (`CharacterStyle.GalacticMark`) carry the team's mark (style guide, "Life", "The Galactic mark") on the chest of their uniform: a lozenge in the mark's yellow round its dark core, painted crisp in the middle of the chest, about a quarter of it across.
- **The first trainers** (plan 11 · C2), each our own take, known at a glance on the board by hair, hat and colours: the school kids in navy with a white collar (the boy in a bowl cut and grey shorts with a satchel, the girl with a ponytail tied in red and a red skirt); the Bug Catcher in a green cap and a short-sleeved green shirt; the Camper in a knitted khaki beanie and shorts with a pack; the Picnicker with a ponytail, a yellow top and green shorts; the twins, little girls in bunches and pink dresses; the boy with his game, little, curly-haired, in an orange striped top; the Hiker big, bald and bearded, in a rust-brown shirt with a big pack; the Worker in a yellow hard hat and an orange top banded for safety; the grunts in the team's pale uniform with its mark and their teal bowls, the woman in a short grey skirt; the Cyclists in helmets (red, and pink with a ponytail) and striped short-sleeved tops over black shorts; the Battle Girl in a red headband with a ponytail, a white top and black shorts, barefoot; the Ace Trainers in navy and white (the man's hair spiky, the woman's in a ponytail with a navy skirt); the Black Belt with a white headband; the Psychic in a long violet coat with swept violet hair; the Ninja Boy all in navy under a navy bandana with a red scarf; the Ruin Maniac bearded and in glasses under a tan hard hat with a pack; the Artist with a moustache under a maroon beret in a cream smock.
- **The second trainers' parts** (plan 11 · C3): clothes, a hat, things held and a build. A **lab coat** is the coat in white over the shirt and a **dress** the skirt in the top's colour: values of a look, not parts. **Overalls** (`CharacterStyle.Overalls`) are a bib from the waist to the chest and two straps over the shoulders in the trousers' colour; an **apron** (`Apron`, its colour) a bib over the chest and a panel from the waist to the knees that swings with the skirt's bone; a **vest** (`Vest`, its colour) the body and shoulders in its colour with the shirt showing down a narrow open front, the sleeves the shirt's; a **robe** (`Robe`) the coat closed and down to the ankles with a sash round the waist in the accent colour; a **swimsuit** (`Swimsuit`) bare arms from the shoulder and bare legs, the suit the top's and the bottom's colours (a man's top in the skin's colour is bare-chested), no collar. A **cowboy hat** (`Headwear.Cowboy`) is a tall crown with a dent along its top, a band at its foot and a wide brim curled up at the sides, no wider at the front than a fedora's. A person may **hold** one thing (`CharacterStyle.Holds`, `HeldProp`, its colour `PropColor` or the thing's own): a **rod** in the right hand, pointing out ahead and up, a reel near the grip; a **parasol** over the right shoulder, its canopy a shallow dome a head and a half across behind and over the head, a scalloped rim in the accent colour; a **guitar** slung across the front, its body at the hips and its neck up to the left shoulder; a **net** in the right hand, a pole with a hoop and a pale bag; a **pickaxe** over the right shoulder; a **float ring** round the waist, striped. What is held is part of the body and moves with its bone (the hand, the body, the hips). The **elder** build (`BodyBuild.Elder`) is an adult nine tenths as tall with the head carried a little forward.
- **The last trainers' parts** (plan 11 · C4): a **visor** (`Headwear.Visor`) is a band round the brow in the band's colour with a peak at its front in the hat's colour, the hair showing above it as it is; a **wide hat** (`Headwear.WideHat`) is a low round crown with a band at its foot and a wide brim all round, the brim lower at the back than at the front so the eyes still show from the field's camera; a **camera** (`HeldProp.Camera`) is a television camera on the right shoulder, a box half a head long with a lens pointing ahead, moving with the body.
- **The last trainers** (plan 11 · C4), each our own take: the Sailor big in a white cap and a striped white top over navy; the Skiers in beanies and bright jackets with goggles (glasses in yellow); the Policeman in a navy cap with a gold band and navy uniform; the Roughneck bald with dark glasses and a black top; the Idol in pink with a bow; the Socialite in a wide white hat and a white dress; the Rich Boy in a navy blazer and shorts with swept fair hair; the Waiter in a black vest over white, the Waitress and the Maid in black with white aprons, the Maid with a white headband; the Reporter in a red jacket and the Cameraman in a cap with his camera; the Mart's woman in its blue apron; the Gym's guide in sunglasses and a pale striped shirt; the Jogger in a visor and a yellow vest; the snow's people and Ace Trainers in beanies, warm coats and scarves; a baby, the littlest of all; the Mystery Gift's deliveryman in a green cap and uniform with his bag; the Battle Frontier's attendants in its green and gold, and the Wi-Fi Plaza's in blue under visors; the game's director in black with glasses and a grey beard; and, only on the battle's platform, the Dragon Tamer in a black cape with red, the Pokémon Rangers in red and blue (the man in a cap, the woman in a visor), and the PI in a tan fedora and a long tan coat.
- **Unova's classes** (plan 22 · U12), each a design of our own on the kit's parts, no part new, named as Black 2's object looks are so the field finds them by itself (a name another region's objects use is taken by the look of that region's own: the doctor is `physician`, since Sinnoh's `doctor` is the professor, and the dancer `street_dancer`, since HeartGold's is a Kimono Girl): the preschoolers little, the boy in a yellow cap with a satchel, the girl in bunches and a pink smock under a pale apron, and their nursery aide in a mint apron over a coral top; the janitor in a green cap and grey overalls with a moustache; the doctor in a white coat over teal scrubs; the baker in a white bandana over a ponytail and a cream apron; the depot agent in a navy coat buttoned up and a navy cap banded in brass; the harlequin masked, in violet and white stripes; the dancer in a lime vest and a pink headband; the musician in a teal coat and a beanie with a guitar; the backers in red and white stripes (the boy in a cap, the girl in a ponytail and a skirt); the smasher in a white visor and skirt banded green; the hoopster tall in an orange striped jersey and a white headband; the striker in a green striped shirt and white shorts; the linebacker broad in a red helmet; the infielder in a navy cap and pinstripes; the backpackers with packs, the man in a green beanie and the woman under a wide sun hat with braids; the woman of the Veterans in an olive coat with silver hair in a bun and the woman of the Psychics in a lilac coat with long dark violet hair; Team Plasma's women grunts in the men's slate hood with a skirt, the team's sages in long slate robes sashed in pale gold, and its former members in plain wheat-coloured clothes (the woman in an apron). In battle each class stands as its look (two looks by the person's, as the Ace Trainers), Hugh as himself among the rivals.
- **Unova's last named people** (plan 22 · U12): original looks of ours for their roles, on the kit's parts with nothing new and no attempt at any other game's design: Opelucid's Gym Leader an old scholar (`opelucid_leader`: short grey hair, round glasses, bushy brows, a slate jacket banded in copper over slate trousers); Humilau's a young man of the beach (`humilau_leader`: sun-browned, sandy swept hair, a coral and white striped shirt, navy shorts); the young Boss Trainer of the tournament's hall a boy (`tower_boss`: a teal cap over brown hair, an olive jacket, a little short); and the three brothers who keep Striaton's café alike in a cream shirt under a deep teal apron, told apart by their hair alone (`cafe_brother_a` short and brown, `_b` swept and black, `_c` curly and copper). Each stands in battle as in the field.
- **Faces in battle**: a smooth texture (128 px, anti-aliased, cut out round the features) laid over the front of the head: dark rim, an iris lighter toward the bottom, a pupil, two white highlights (upper left, lower right), a heavier upper lid, lash flicks for long lashes, brows only where the bangs leave the brow bare, a small mouth (under the moustache, if any), and glasses where the look has them: a round rim in the frame's colour round each eye, a bridge between them and a short temple outward, there with the eyes shut too. Blush is painted on the cheeks of the model itself. Expressions: neutral, happy, surprised, sad, angry, each also with the eyes shut for blinking.
- **A trainer back on the platform** (plan 11 · C13): a trainer of the other side who speaks in battle walks back from beside their platform (7 units out along the way they ran off) to its edge, the first or only trainer on the right and the second of two on the left (2.4 + 0.35 out from the platform's middle, 1.1 behind it), in 0.4 s eased to a stop, as the line appears, at the scale they stood at before the send-out (2.3, or 2.1 for one of two). There they turn to the field, a little toward the player's side (0.35 rad), with the line's face (its `mood`: one of the five expressions above) and, once arrived, its gesture played once (its `act`: one of the emotes of the field's "Animation", a jump lifting them eight texels' worth of their scale). Once the line is read they walk off the way they came in 0.4 s; a line said while they are still going turns them back from where they are. With the Battle Scene off they speak from off the platform.
- **Pixel faces on sprites**: a smooth face shrunk to sprite size turns to mush, so each baked frame gets a face stamped where the point between the eyes lands: eyes two texels wide and three tall (lash row, then a white highlight on the upper left beside the iris, then iris), three texels apart; a mouth of one to three texels two rows below; one eye in profile, none from behind. A blink is a two-texel line. Glasses are a one-texel rim round each eye (four texels wide, five tall) with the bridge between them on the eyes' top row, or round the one eye in profile with two texels of temple behind it; the eyes are stamped over them.
- **Light on faces**: the normals of the front of the head are bent mostly upward (toward `0, 0.75, 0.66`), so a face lights evenly under any light from above and the edge of the shade never splits it, in profile too.
- **Light on hair**: the hair's normals are bent most of the way (70 %) toward those of the smooth mass it fills: a sphere round the head, stretched into a capsule down the back for long hair. The shade's edge and the sheen then run cleanly across the head instead of breaking into a blotch on every lock; the locks still show in the silhouette, the outline and the grooves between them.
- **Animation** (`CharacterAnimation`, 19 bones): idle breathing with the arms lowered from the sculpted A-pose; walk and run cycles in which the hips drop as the legs spread, so a foot stays on the ground, with arms swinging against the legs (the run leans forward with the elbows bent); the ledge hop; emotes (wave, surprised, nod, cheer, and since plan 11 · C11 bow, shrug, point and laugh). A bow bends the body forward from the waist with the head down and the arms hanging; a shrug lifts both shoulders, brings the forearms out with the hands open and tilts the head; a point holds the right arm straight out ahead at the shoulder's height, the head turned a little after it; a laugh tips the head back and shakes the shoulders. Every emote eases in from the idle and ends at it. A jump on the spot is the ledge hop's clip played where the person stands, lifted eight texels at its height. What the field plays on cue is a script's (`act`, `mood`; `docs/scripts.md`): a gesture is an emote played over the idle as many times as asked, a mood the face held until the script changes it or the place is come to again; the start under a trainer's "!" still wins over both. Hair, bag and skirt swing a beat behind the body. Raised hands stay beside the big head, never behind it ("\o/", not straight up).
- **Stances** (plan 11 · C7, `Stance`): someone may stand, **sit** on a seat, **sit on the ground** or **lie**. Sitting, the hips drop to the knees' height with the thighs level and pointing ahead, the shins hang straight down, the hands rest on the lap and a skirt or a coat's tails lie forward over the thighs; on the ground the hips drop to it and the legs lie out ahead, a little bent, the hands beside the hips; lying, the body lies on its back along the way it faces (on a bed, the head at its head, which is north), the arms at the sides. Each breathes as the idle does and blinks; nobody walks, hops or plays a gesture while not standing (a gesture waits until they stand again). Whoever stands on a seat's tile takes its stance unless they are given one: a bench, a chair or a sofa seats them, a floor cushion seats them on the ground, a bed lays them down. A sitter is drawn on the seat, never in it: their card is lifted until the hips are at the seat's height (a chair 14 texels, a sofa or a bench 13, a cushion 5) and moved 0.28 of a tile along the way they face, so the legs hang in front of the seat's edge; a lier in a bed is under its covers: their card stands on the pillow, sunk until the neck is at the mattress's top (14 texels), so the bed hides the body and the head lies on the pillow (lying flat seen from the sprite's 24° would make a body too short on the field's screen, and tilted to make up for it a body faces the camera as if standing). A sitter keeps the seat's facing when spoken to, and neither wanders nor looks about.
- **Swimming and fishing** (plan 11 · C8): three more stances. Someone on open deep water who isn't on a deck or wading **swims** without being told (`Stance.Swim`): treading water upright, the arms sweeping out and round in a breaststroke a stroke every 1.2 seconds of the field's clock, the legs a little apart and bent; the sprite keeps only what is above the water line, the shoulders' height (the rest is cleared, so nothing shows through the water), and the card is sunk until that line is at the water's surface. A ring of the field's own (the surfer's) spreads behind them at every stroke, timed by the field's clock and their name so it is the same every run. The player **fishing** holds the rod (the rig is the player's look holding one, `PLAYER+Rod`): from the cast to the bite the rod is out over the water in both hands, the right arm forward and the rod pointing out and up (`Stance.Fish`, two breaths); on the bite the arms pull back in three frames, from the rod out to the rod held high (`Stance.Tug`), until the catch is told.
- **Sprites** (`CharacterSprites`): 40×58 texels, orthographic, seen from 24° above, studio light from the upper left, the pixel face stamped on, then a one-texel outline tinted from the neighbouring colour (`PixelCanvas.OutlinePass`). Frames for each of the four facings: 2 idle breaths, 8 walk, 8 run, 3 hop (the jump on the spot shows them too), 2 breaths for each stance (strips `sit`, `sitground` and `lie`, plan 11 · C7, and `fish`; C8's `swim` has 4 frames of a stroke and `tug` 3 of a pull) and 6 per emote (strips `wave`, `surprised`, `nod`, `cheer`, `bow`, `shrug`, `point` and `laugh`; the throw is drawn in battle only); blinking and expressions are frames of their own, so a mood is a face on any strip. Drawn as upright cards lit by the scene (sky × 0.62 + sun × 0.78), casting real shadows, with a soft contact blob under the feet. A trainer who spots the player starts (the surprised emote) under the "!".
- **A diagonal step** (plan 30 · FM2, where a region walks eight ways): the player slides across to the diagonal tile in a straight line, √2 times as long as a step so the speed over the ground is a step's, the walk cycle running on. Until FM3 and FM4 draw eight facings the sprite keeps one of the four: the cardinal of the two the player already faced, else the vertical one (stepping north-east while facing east, they face east; from facing south or west, north). A follower treads the same diagonal facing its vertical part. Nobody else ever moves slantwise.
- **Hand-drawn overrides**: a 40×58 PNG at `overrides/sprites/<TYPE>/<facing>_<strip>_<frame>[_blink][_<expression>].png` next to the game (for example `PLAYER/down_walk_3.png`) replaces that baked frame.
- **Pokémon in the field** (plan 10 · F1, `FieldSprites`): baked from the Pokémon's own 3D model as the people's sprites are (orthographic, 24° above, the same studio light, a one-texel outline from `OutlinePass`), at the same 32 texels to the tile, so sizes compare: a Pokémon stands `0.3 + 0.75 ×` its height in metres tiles tall (from 0.4 to 1.9; the player is about 1.7), a Machop a little over half the player and a Steelix taller. The card is square, 32 texels under 0.7 tiles, 48 to 1.6, 64 above (`FieldSprites.ClassOf`), and the model is shrunk until it fits it from every side. Its lowest point stands on the card's bottom row but one and a half, which is the ground. Four facings with two idle frames each (breathed out and in on the idle's beat); no blink, no stamped face (its eyes are the model's). Drawn like the people: an upright card lit and casting, a contact blob three quarters of a person's. While a sprite isn't baked yet a Poké Ball stands in its place. A Pokémon of the map that moves about walks (plan 10 · F2): each facing has a walk strip of eight frames on the same card, baked the first time that Pokémon is seen on the move (`FieldSprites.PrepareWalk`), one stride of its body plan's walk (the *walk* clip, under "Pokémon (version 2, G7)") over two tiles, as the people's eight frames are, so frame `floor(8 × frac(WalkCycle))` shows; until its strip is baked it hops two texels on its steps and shows its two idle frames in turn, the second while it is off the ground, a hop to each tile. A Pokémon of the team out of its ball (plan 06 · R16c: in Amity Square, a step behind the player; plan 10 · F3: the lead, anywhere the follower may be) is a Pokémon of the map like any other: the same card, the same blob, the same walk, and it keeps to the tile the player has just left, so it is never drawn over the player but where the player walks north with it behind, when the top of a tall one crosses the player's feet as anyone's would who stood there. On the water the player rides the Pokémon whose Surf it is: its sprite from the side or the back the player faces, a fifth of it under the water, the rider sitting a little over halfway up what shows; the round swimmer stands in while the sprite bakes. A legendary that waits where it is met may be drawn at an alpha's size, its card half as large again (an overlay person's `scale`, 1 to 1.5, never more; plan 23: Resolution Cave's Zygarde, whose 5 m the 1.9-tile cap had left barely taller than the player), its foot where its own size's would be. A square PNG of 32, 48 or 64 at `overrides/sprites/pokemon/<NAME>/<facing>_idle_<frame>.png` replaces a baked frame (both frames of a facing are needed), and eight at `<facing>_walk_<frame>.png` a facing's walk strip.
- **On a ride** (plan 23, wave 91; `TravelMode.Riding`, Alola's Ride Pager on land): the player sits on the ride's own field sprite (Tauros, Stoutland or Mudsdale, the Pokémon of the map's card at its facing) as a surfer sits on the Pokémon whose Surf it was: the rider's feet `round(0.45 × (rows − 1.5))` rows above the ground (`WorldRenderer.RideSeat`), the legs still (no walk cycle) and the ride's card a sixteenth of a tile nearer the camera than the rider, so its back covers their shoes. Moving, the two rise a texel together on every other eighth of a second (its gallop); standing, they keep still. Until the ride's sprite is baked the player stands on their own feet and nothing is drawn under them; on land no swimmer ever stands in.
- **The follower** (plan 10 · F3, the options' FOLLOWER row, off unless chosen): the team's first Pokémon that can stand walks a tile behind the player (two for one drawn taller than 1.3 tiles, as in Amity Square), drawn as above. It is put back in its ball, with no picture of its own, while the player surfs, cycles or dives, in a cave still dark, below ground, in Amity Square (whose walk is its own) and while someone travels with the player; it comes out again behind the player as soon as none of those holds. Its steps leave prints, leaves and splashes where the player's would (`FieldLife.Footstep`, at a walk: it raises no running dust), and over a ledge it hops a tile behind the player, a hop of the same height, landing in the same two puffs of dust. Coming out of its ball it lands in those puffs too. Spoken to (plan 10 · F4) it turns to the player, a bubble over its head (a heart, a note, an ellipsis or a "!" by how it feels) and its cry. **Its mood** (plan 35 · BN4, the care book's): spoken to, its bubble is its mood's (dots glum or low, a note cheerful, a heart elated, its band's calm), and a glum one turns its back on the player rather than to them. **Bubbles of its own**: after a battle (a note for a win, a heart if elated, dots for a loss; a heart for a level), an evolution (a heart), a new place or the weather's turn (a question, or dots while glum or low), the bubble its moment brings, over its head for 0.9 s, at most once in 24 of its steps; a moment that comes inside those steps waits for them and the latest wins.
- **Wild Pokémon in sight** (plan 10 · F5, the options' WILD POKéMON row, HIDDEN unless chosen): with VISIBLE the grass and the water stop rolling and up to six wild Pokémon stand in the open area's grass and water in view, drawn as Pokémon of the map with their walk, wandering a tile at a time within three tiles of where they came out, half submerged on water as the surfer's mount is (a fifth under). One appears with no effect (the grass's own leaves from `FieldLife.Footstep` under it) and is gone when its chunk leaves the view. Touched, it is met in the flash of the way into a wild battle the grass would have given. **In the place of a day's outbreak** (plan 38 · EX1, `Overworld/Outbreaks.cs`): up to ten stand in the grass instead of six (`FieldPokemon.MostInOutbreak`), and eight in ten of them are the outbreak's one species, because they are drawn from the grass's slots 0 to 5, which that species fills; nothing else about them changes (no shimmer, no ring, no mark: the place itself is told by the Town Map's report once plan 38 · EX2 builds it, and by the toast that says thirty are met). A crowd is a room's worth of one kind, so it keeps off the tiles of the player's two-tile ring as any one does and each walks on its own timer; the ten are not a formation.
- **A bright Pokémon in sight** (plan 38 · EX3, `FieldLife.Shimmer`): once every two seconds of the field's clock a ring of warm light (`255,214,120`) spreads under its tile and four sparks of the same colour rise beside its card, one after another, a fifth of a second apart; nothing else of it changes. As it comes out in battle it shimmers once (`CueKind.Bright`, `MoveFx.BrightShimmer`): a warm glow (`255,206,110`) swells and fades round its middle on the shiny sparkle's timing, with six small glows turning round it and rising; no stars and no sound, so it is never taken for a shiny one, and a shiny one that is also bright shows only its sparkle.

- **Apricorn trees** (plan 20 · J6, `ApricornArt`): a tree is a card 32 texels wide and 48 tall standing on its tile, drawn with the field's things (`DrawThings`) and in the way like a person. A trunk 4 texels thick at the top and 6 at the foot (`112,78,52`, lit on its left edge `150,110,74`, shaded on its right `78,54,40`) with two roots spreading, under a broad round canopy 29 texels across and 30 high in three greens (leaf `70,150,66`, light `120,196,96` on the upper left, shade `44,104,52` under and on the lower right) with notches of the dark one round its rim. In fruit it has three Apricorns hanging in it, each a round fruit 7 texels across in its colour (`Data/apricorns.json`: Red `214,62,56`, Blue `66,126,214`, Yellow `240,204,70`, Green `92,176,78`, Pink `240,130,176`, White `238,238,244`, Black `70,72,88`), lit on the upper left, shaded on the lower right, on a dark stalk; bare it is the same tree without them, one card for all colours. It turns bare the moment its Apricorn is picked and bears again from the next calendar day. The tree and its colours are ours: HeartGold's trees are a model per colour, and nothing of it is copied.
- **The Apricorn Box** (plan 20 · J6, `ModernUi.DrawApricornBox`): the Fashion Case's two panels. At the left the seven colours one to a row, each a round fruit on its row's left (the colour above, a rim a shade darker, a glint on the upper left and a short stalk; paler when none is held), its name and `×count` at the right, the colour under the cursor a Selection pill; at the right the colour under the cursor large on a pale disc (130 across), its name, `×count of 99`, the ball Kurt makes of it, and under a rule Kurt's order: "Nothing ordered.", the balls in the making (ready tomorrow) or ready at his house.
- **Berry patches** (plan 06 · R14a, `BerryArt`): a patch of soft soil is a card 32 texels wide and 40 tall standing on its tile, drawn like the field's things (`DrawThings`). Its foot is a mound of loam 28 texels across and 10 high, lit from the upper left in three tones with seven crumbs of darker earth; mulch darkens it by a step and its crumbs turn to pale straw. What grows in it goes stage by stage: a seed showing in the top of the mound, a sprout of two leaves, a stem of four, a bush of six leaves on three stems with three flowers (pale petals round a yellow eye) and the same bush with three berries five texels across, each lit on one side, shaded on the other and hung from a dark stalk. A berry's colour is its strongest flavour's, as a Poffin's is: spicy red, dry blue, sweet pink, bitter green, sour yellow. The original's tile of soil and the plants' sprites are not copied: the stages and the colours are its numbers; the art is ours.

### Grown characters

From Kalos on, people are not chibi (plan 30, the user's ruling of 2026-11-02). They are made with a **second kit beside the chibi one** (plan 30, decision 12), in `Graphics/CharacterModels.Grown.cs`: the same SDF kit, the same skinned programs and materials, the same rules of layered clothes, hair in locks and hats on the hair as "Characters" above, re-made at a grown body's scale. The chibi kit, its looks, its meshes and their cache keys, and every shot of a chibi person are untouched by it. Plan 30 · FM4 builds it in two parts: part a (wave 91) the body (the five builds, the skeleton and the sculpting helpers below), part b (wave 93) every hair cut, hat and garment at grown scale and the grown faces; the user judges the turntables after part b.

- **Which kit a look is made in.** A look says its kit (`kit` in `characters.json`, `CharacterStyle.Kit`, `Chibi` unless it says `Grown`), or is asked for in a build by its name: `<look>~<build>` (`PLAYER~Teen`; `CharacterStyle.For` reads it, beside an outfit's `@` and a held thing's `+`, in any order), which is how a region's kit reaches the player who crosses into it (plan 30, decision 4: `CharacterStyle.InKit(look, kit)` names the look in the build its own build stands for there). A build belongs to one kit, and a look's build is read in the kit it is made in: `Kid` is `Teen` in the grown kit and `Teen` is `Kid` in the chibi, `Adult` and `Grown`, `Little` and `Child`, `Elder` and `GrownElder` the same, `GrownBroad` `Adult` (`CharacterStyle.BuildIn`). A look asked in the grown kit is the table's `grown` look of that name where it has one (FM10 wrote them, for Kalos's whole cast: "Kalos's cast", below), else its own (`CharacterStyles.Get(name, kit)`).
- **The five builds** (model units before the look's `height` scales them; the crown is the top of the skull, which the hair lies over; the head's size is half its height, from the chin to the crown, and its middle is at the eyes):

  | Build | For | Crown | Head (half its height at its middle) | Heads tall | Hip joints | Shoulders across (joint to joint) | Girth |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | `Teen` | the player, the rival, students | 1.485 | 0.105 at 1.38 | 7 | 0.76 | 0.30 | 0.88 |
  | `Grown` | most adults | 1.585 | 0.105 at 1.48 | 7.5 | 0.82 | 0.36 | 1 |
  | `GrownBroad` | hikers, black belts, sailors | 1.64 | 0.11 at 1.53 | 7.5 | 0.83 | 0.44 | 1.22 |
  | `Child` | youngsters, lasses, little children | 1.14 | 0.115 at 1.025 | 5 | 0.53 | 0.24 | 0.82 |
  | `GrownElder` | the elderly | 1.465 | 0.105 at 1.36 | 7 | 0.76 | 0.32 | 0.92 |

  With its hair a grown figure stands about 1.5, 1.6, 1.65, 1.15 and 1.48 tall, plan 30's heights. The elder carries the head 0.04 forward and the upper back rounded; the broad build's shoulders are wider and its girth thickens every limb and the neck. The child's head is a little larger, its hips lower and its limbs fuller than plan 30's first numbers (0.11, 0.56, 0.76), which made a small adult of it on the first turntables.
- **The rest of the frame follows from the table**: the chin at the head's middle less its half height; the shoulder joints 0.9 of the head's half height under the chin, and the neck's foot (between the collarbones) 0.02 over them; down the torso from the shoulders to the hip joints, the rib cage's middle at 0.30 of the way, the chest's own joint at 0.45 and the waist at 0.70; the upper arm 0.65 of that torso long, the forearm 0.51 and the hand 0.58 of the upper arm; the ankle 0.045 of the crown high and the knee halfway between it and the hip joint; the foot 0.152 of the crown long, a fifth of it behind the ankle. The arms are sculpted in an A-pose 20° out from the body, the hands turned in, the legs straight down a little apart, so arms and body stay apart for clean weights; the chibi idle lowers them by 15°.
- **The skeleton** (26 bones, within the skinned programs' 32): the chibi kit's 19 at their numbers, and seven appended: `Neck` 19, `Chest` 20, the collarbones `ClavicleL` 21 and `ClavicleR` 22, the toes `ToeL` 23 and `ToeR` 24, and `CoatTail` 25 (the back of a long coat, apart from the skirt's bone). The spine is in two: `Spine` (2) at the waist carries `Chest` at the bottom of the rib cage, which carries the neck, the collarbones and the bag; the head (3) hangs from the neck, each upper arm from its collarbone, each toe from its foot, the coat's tail from the hips. A bone may hang from one later in the list (`Skeleton.Reparent`, evaluated parents first). The chibi clips set bones 0 to 18 and leave the seven at rest, so a grown rig can follow every chibi clip, and a chibi rig is posed exactly as before; since plan 30 · FM5 a grown rig is posed by grown clips that move the seven ("Grown animation", below).
- **Meshing**: cells of 1/144 (`GrownMeshCell`), against the chibi's 1/112, since a grown limb is a third as thick; plan 30's 1/160 put the broad build's body at 64,000 vertices, at the edge of the 65,535 a mesh can index before part b's locks go on it, where 1/144 leaves it at about 52,000 (a grown adult about 41,000, the teen and the elder 34,000, the child 24,000). The bone weights blend over 0.03, but a shoulder's reach further into the chest round it (plan 30 · FM10): the deltoid's weight over 0.09 (`GrownShoulderReach`) and a sleeve's over 0.2 (`GrownSleeveReach`, the garment lying further out round the chest), and a shape blended over one that reaches far blends its own weight as far (`SdfPrimitive.WeightReach`), so a raised arm's bend is spread down the side of the chest and no edge of the body or its outline stretches past four times its length under the arm in a wave, a cheer or a throw (over 0.03 a hundred and more did; a garment's deep crease still stretches a few dozen at the top of a throw, linear skinning's own limit there). The outline is a shell 0.008 out, meshed in cells a quarter larger. No part is thinner across than two and a half cells but the thumb (two): a lock, a strap or a finger of part b keeps to it. A painted edge is soft over a cell and a half (`GrownPaintEdge`): a crisp paint steps along every cell of a body this fine (the chibi kit's hems are geometry, with outlines of their own, and part b's will be too).
- **The torso**: a pelvis on the hips, a waist on the spine and a rib cage on the chest, each an ellipsoid, joined smoothly; the rib cage's half width 0.78 of the shoulders' half span, the waist 0.86 of the rib cage's and the pelvis 0.92 (a child's waist 0.94), their depths 0.66, 0.80 and 0.66 of their widths. A **curved** figure (`curved` in `characters.json`; left out, it follows the look's long lashes, which the table gives its women and girls) has a waist 0.76 of the rib cage, a pelvis 1.06 of it, the knees a little closer and a bust on the chest; a child's figure is never curved (plan 30's first shares, 0.68 and 1.12, made an hourglass of it). From up the back of the neck a slope runs down and out to each shoulder on its collarbone (the trapezius), and the shoulder's round is the upper arm's own (a deltoid on the upper arm's bone, no wider than the arm's top by more than a sixth).
- **Limbs** (the radii at a girth of 1; the build's girth scales them): each a tapered round cone from joint to joint, the upper arm 0.044 to 0.030 with its deltoid at the shoulder, the forearm 0.033 near the elbow to 0.024 at the wrist, the thigh 0.074 to 0.046 from inside the pelvis (its top below the waist) with its front fuller (the muscle's swell) tapering into the knee, the shin 0.050 at the calf (a swell at the back of its upper third) to 0.031 at the ankle. Elbows and knees keep their volume when they bend: a joint sphere a little fuller than both segments on the lower bone (the knee 0.047, the elbow 0.032), so the weights pass between the two over the joint.
- **The legs apart**: the hip joints 0.66 of the pelvis's half width out (and never nearer the middle than 0.056 of the girth and 0.022, which sets a child's thick legs apart), the knees 1.05 of that (0.95 for a curved figure) and the ankles 1.12 (1.08); each leg's shapes joined over small blends (0.012 to 0.015). The outline's shell stands a little out from the body, and where the two legs come nearer each other than the weights' blend and the shell's thickness twice over, its inner side takes weights from the other leg and tears away into a fin in a stride; and a **seat**, a piece of the pelvis between the tops of the thighs on the hips' bone made after both legs, keeps the crotch moving with the pelvis rather than with both thighs at once. A stride, a run and a bow tear nothing (`GrownKitTests`); sitting turns the thighs a quarter turn and stretches the hip's crease, as any skin bent so far by blended weights does, which plan 30 · FM5's grown sitting clip answers.
- **The neck** is a round cone of radius 0.055 at its foot and 0.05 under the skull at a girth of 1, from the neck's foot up and back to under the skull behind the jaw, on its own bone; so the jaw's underside shows above the throat, and the neck never blends into the face.
- **The head** (sizes in the head's half height, from its middle): the skull an ellipsoid 0.20 up and 0.12 back of the middle, 0.74, 0.80 and 0.88 across, up and deep; the face's plate below its front, the cheeks and the upper jaw; the jaw, up from its angle under each ear and forward from it to a chin that stands 0.74 forward; cheekbones; the lips' soft swell; a soft brow; the ears at the middle of the sides, from the eyes' height to the nose's foot, tilted back; **a nose**, which a chibi face never had: a bridge from between the eyes down to a round tip about 1.0 forward of the middle, and two wings (alae) beside it. Every part of the face is joined over a wide blend (0.1 to 0.2 of the half height): a tight crease reads as an ink line under the toon light. No eye and no mouth is sculpted: the face is painted over the front of the head (part b), and its anchor is the surface between the eyes at the head's middle, the eyes' centres 0.30 of the half height either side of it (a fifth of the head's width apart).
- **Hands** are mittens: a flat palm facing the body, the fingers one long mass a little curled toward it, and a thumb along the palm's forward edge, on the hand's bone; a hand is 0.58 of the upper arm long. **Feet** carry their toes: the heel and the instep on the foot's bone, the ball and the toes on the toe's, from the ball three quarters of the way from the heel; a shoe is the foot's own surface in the shoe's colour, flat on the ground, with its sole painted in the sole's; over bare legs its collar shows round the ankle, and trousers fall over it.
- **The body under the clothes** (the base layer): the trousers, the shoes, the hands, the head and bare skin are the body's own, as in every kit ("Layered clothes"); what is over the torso is painted on the body from the waist up to a round neckline at the neck's foot (under a garment its lining, a swimsuit's top, a bare chest's skin); shorts end at mid-thigh, a swimsuit leaves the legs bare under the bottom's colour on the hips; the trousers end over the shoe in a hem a shade darker. The hair is one cap over the skull in the hair's colour (none when bald, a bowl's own mass for a bowl, a mask's hood under a mask), thicker on top and at the back, its edge a rounded rim (each cut over a blend of 0.1 to 0.12 of the half height): the hairline across the forehead 0.55 over the eyes, the temples bare at the eyes' height, the sideburns ending in front of each ear at its middle, and a line from over the ears down to the nape. Part a stopped there; part b (below) lays the locks over it, the hats on it and the clothes over the body.
- **The frame's limits**: a grown figure in its A-pose is no wider than 1.0 (the broad build is 0.96) and no taller than its build's crown and 0.12: every hair cut and every hat stays within it (`GrownKitTests`), and only what is held may stand over it (a parasol's canopy).
- **Light**: the head's skin normals are bent 60 % toward those of a smooth egg round the head (from the crown to the chin, 0.74, 1.02 and 0.88 of the half height), so the face lights as one form, and then the front of the face half as far as a chibi face's toward `0, 0.75, 0.66`, so the shade's edge never splits it; both bends fade where the neck's weight takes over and where the nose and the ears stand out of the egg, so they still shade. The hair's normals are bent 80 % toward a sphere round the skull, stretched into a capsule down the back for hair that falls below the head (long hair, a ponytail), as "Light on hair" says but further (a grown head's locks are finer beside its cells, and at 70 % a twentieth of the hair's neighbouring normals turned more than 14°); a moustache and a beard keep theirs, lit with the face.

The rest is part b's (wave 93): the hair, the hats, the clothes, what grows on a face, what is held and the painted face, each the chibi kit's part re-made at a grown head's and body's size. Sizes on the head are in the head's half height (`R`, the table's "Head"); the hair's **cap** is an ellipsoid over the skull, its middle 0.26 R over and 0.14 R behind the head's middle, 0.86, 0.86 and 1.0 R across, up and through, and what lies on the hair follows it, not a sphere.

- **Hair in locks** (`GrownLock`): tapering tubes laid along the cap's own surface from the crown's whorl (the hairline for swept hair; just under a hat's edge under a hat) to their tips, sunk 0.08 R into the cap with their tips lifting off it, about 0.2 R across at the root and never thinner at the tip than two and a half cells, joined over a blend of 0.03 R so a groove shows between neighbours. **Bangs** are four pointed locks over the forehead ending a little over the brows, never over them (a grown face's brows show), the outer two higher; locks over the top, three down each side ending over the ear, behind it and at the nape, and one down the back. **Short** is those; **long** adds a smooth mass behind the head and the nape and four locks from the back of the head to the shoulder blades, slanting back to clear the upper back however deep the chest is, full to their ends (0.15 R) and close enough to join down the back, the outer two ending a little higher so the ends make one soft curve (thin, wide-set tips hung apart like fingers), and a lock framing each cheek from the temple to below the jaw; it rests on the back, so what is above the nape moves with the head and what lies on the back with the chest, stretching between them as the neck does (on the hair's bone, which a bow turns past the head, its outline tore away from the back); **spiky** is ten tufts from the crown standing up and swept back, 0.38 to 0.55 R long, with locks over the ears and the nape; **swept** is combed back from the hairline in sleek locks lying close to the cap (0.17 R, sunk 0.1 R, no lift), the forehead bare; a **bun** is the swept cut gathered into a knot of 0.38 R high on the back of the head with a twist round its foot; a **ponytail** is the short cut with a tail tied high at the back in the band's colour (below the hat's edge under a hat) falling to the shoulder blades on the hair's bone; **braids** are the short cut with two plaits from under the ears over the front of the shoulders to the chest, five beads each, tied in the band's colour with a tuft below; **bunches** are the short cut with two tails tied at the sides a little behind the ears, falling close beside the head to below the jaw on the hair's bone, tied in the band's colour; **curls** are round curls 0.21 R across over the cap in rings, a row at the fringe over the brows, none over the face; an **afro** is a round mass set back and up from the cap so its front meets the hairline (the brows bare), with round curls on its surface; a **bowl** is one mass a little fuller than the cap cut by a plane straight across the brow, just over the brows, sloping down past the tops of the ears to the nape; **bald** is no cap, a fringe of short locks (0.12 R) two rows deep and joined, down to the nape, lying close round the back of the skull from behind one ear to behind the other (one row across the back read as a headband).
- **Hats** (`GrownHat`) sit on the hair, never in it, the chibi kit's fourteen at a grown head's size: their crowns over the cap and its locks, and under a beret, a cap, a fedora, a helmet, a hard hat, a beanie, a bandana, a cowboy hat, a wide hat or a mask no lock grows over the top; under a bandana, a beanie, a helmet, a hard hat or a mask no bangs show either (each comes down to the brow). A **brim or a peak** is a flat disc with a fully rounded edge, at least 0.1 R (and 1.6 cells) half thick (`Brim`): an ellipsoid's edge thins to nothing and breaks up in the mesh, which the first turntables showed on every brim. A **band** is a ring at least 0.13 R (1.6 cells) thick blended into what it lies on (`Band`), and a band round the head itself (a headband, a visor's) is an ellipse 0.97 R across and 1.07 R through set 0.18 R behind the head's middle, so it lies on the forehead in front and on the hair at the sides and behind (a circle stood off the head's sides). Brims: a fedora's 1.62 by 1.5 R, a cowboy hat's 1.68 by 1.48 R curled up at the sides, a wide hat's 1.72 by 1.68 R, a hard hat's 1.32 by 1.4 R, no wider to the front than at the sides so the eyes still show from the field's camera; a cap's peak 0.72 by 0.56 R, a helmet's 0.56 by 0.3 R, a visor's 0.62 by 0.4 R. A **mask** is the cap's own hood in the hat's colour, its face cut out from the brows down and its edge round the face painted crisp (the face's wide blends would smudge it), with a crest in the band's colour from the brow to the nape.
- **What grows on a face** (`GrownFacialHair`), in the hair's colour: a **moustache** is two tapered masses under the nose over the upper lip; a **beard** a mass round the chin under the mouth with a sideburn up each side of the jaw to the ear, never over the mouth or the cheeks; **bushy brows** two sculpted ridges over the eyes, where the painted brows would be (which are then not painted). **Blush** is painted on the cheeks under the eyes' outer corners.
- **Clothes** (`GrownClothes`) are a layer of their own over the body, as "Layered clothes" says, meshed in the grown cell with their own outline shell. A garment stands 0.0065 off the body, and the body under it is slimmer (the torso 0.95 across and through, an arm under its sleeve 0.85), so the gap is about two cells; the torso under a garment is the garment's lining (0.62), but behind the neck's foot, where the garment lies close over it, the garment's own colour. The garment's body is the torso's own shapes made bigger, so it rises to a round neck at the neck's foot, with the two slopes' heads joined behind the neck so it runs straight across the nape. Every hem is cut flat with a strip a cell and a half deep a shade darker (0.62) painted at it; sleeves end at the wrist or at 0.62 of the upper arm. A **shirt** ends a little over the hips (over the trousers' top, so shorts and a skirt show whole), a **jacket** just below the hips with a ribbed band (0.86) over its hem and at each cuff, a zip down its front with a metal pull and two pocket flaps over the hips; whatever goes down past the hip joints covers the tops of the thighs, which reach out past the pelvis there (without it the thighs stood out of a jacket's sides like a leotard's, and through a coat's). A **coat**'s tails are on the coat's tail bone (`CoatTail`), two elliptical cones: from the hips, as wide as the thighs' tops, straight down past the hands (0.62 as deep as wide), then flaring below them to just below the knee (0.74 as deep); open over the shirt, its fronts parting in a V from the collar to the waist with the accent colour between them and a line where they meet below, or **buttoned** (a placket line and three buttons in the accent colour), with two pocket flaps low on its tails; a **robe** is the coat closed and down to the ankles with a sash in the accent colour. A **skirt** is on the skirt's bone, from the waist under the shirt's hem flaring to just above the knee, elliptical (0.8 as deep), over the thighs' tops and over bare legs, and **shares each thigh's weight where it lies over it** (plan 30 · FM10: a cone on each thigh's bone inside it, as wide and deep as the skirt's ellipse leaves room for less a cell and a half all round and in the skirt's colour, whose weight reaches 0.12 into the skirt's surface, `GrownSkirtReach`; near half the thigh's where the skirt lies closest, nothing where it lies far), so a leg in a stride carries the skirt over it forward (on the skirt's bone alone the forward thigh came out through its side); under a coat the coat's tails are the skirt. **Overalls**' bib and straps are the trousers' colour, the straps following the clothes' surface over the shoulders; an **apron**'s bib is painted, its panel a piece of its own from the waist to the knee along the front of what is under it, on the skirt's bone, tied at the waist; a **vest** is painted over the garment's body and shoulders with the shirt down its narrow open front. **Stripes** are two bands across the chest and the waist; the team's **mark** is the lozenge in the middle of the chest. A **bag**'s straps follow the clothes' surface round the shoulders with a buckle at each front end, the bag on the back on its bone. A **collar** is a band in the accent colour along the garment's own top where it meets the neck, found by probing down onto the garment round the neck's axis, so it shows all round, higher behind than in front as the garment rises there (a ring round the neck's axis alone sank into the garment in front and stood out of it behind, a bar at the nape on every board); a **scarf** is wrapped along the same line instead, thicker, with its tail hanging in front. A **swimsuit** and a bare chest are the body's own, painted on it, with nothing over them; under a skirt, shorts or a swimsuit the legs are bare (shorts painted to mid-thigh), under a coat or a robe without a skirt they are in trousers.
- **What is held** (`GrownHeld`) is a mesh of its own with its own outline (`CharacterRig.Held`; the chibi kit's body holds it): a parasol's canopy alone is a third of a grown body's vertices, more than the body's mesh or the clothes' has room for (65,535 each), and a thing in the hand drawn apart has its own ink line where it meets the hand. On the bone it moves with, at a grown body's size: a **rod** in the right hand, about a unit long, out ahead and up, a reel near the grip; a **net** in the right hand, a pole with a hoop and a pale bag; a **parasol** from the right hand over the shoulder, its canopy a shallow dome 0.8 across over the head (the top of an ellipsoid 0.12 high, cut off underneath and hollowed, so from below it is a hollow: a flattened ellipsoid whole read as a plate, and cut flat as a tray; the one thing that may stand over the frame), a rim in the accent colour round its edge and a tip over its middle; a **pickaxe** from the right hand over the right shoulder; a **guitar** slung across the front, its body at the hips and its neck up to the left shoulder; a **camera** on the right shoulder's collarbone, half a head long; a **float ring** round the waist, striped.
- **Faces** (`GrownFaces`, 256 pixels, on the face patch): laid over the front of the head as the chibi kit's are, cut out where nothing is painted, so the skin under it shows. **Eyes** are almond-shaped, 0.28 R wide and 0.12 R tall, their middles 0.30 R either side of the anchor at the head's middle, the outer corner a little higher than the inner: a white, an iris 0.071 R round (darker at its top under the lid, lighter toward its foot, a darker ring) with a pupil, cut by the lids, two highlights (up toward the light, and a small one down), the upper lid's line heavier toward the outer corner with the lid's shade over the white's top, a light lower lid and the fold over the eye; a woman's (a look with lashes) has a heavier lid and two lashes flicked out at the outer corner. **Brows** are always drawn but where the look's own are sculpted: a tapered stroke arching over the eye from its heavier inner end, 0.185 R over the eyes, in the hair's colour darkened. The **nose** is the sculpt's, shaded by its own light, with its two nostrils marked under its tip (a shade painted over the tip read as a clown's nose on the first boards). The **mouth** is 0.625 R under the eyes, lips in a rose shade (a man's nearer the skin) with the line between them darker. The five expressions keep the chibi kit's names: happy raises the lower lids into a smile and shows the upper teeth, surprised opens the eyes wider over smaller irises and the mouth in a small round opening, sad droops the upper lids at the outer corners and turns the mouth down, angry presses the lids down at the inner corners and tightens the mouth, each with its brows (raised, the inner ends down in anger and up in sorrow); shut eyes are the lashes' line curving down with the fold over it; speaking, the mouth opens as the chibi kit's does. **Glasses** are a rim round each eye (0.2 by 0.135 R), a bridge and a short temple outward, there with the eyes shut too. The patch is the skin of the head's front moving only with the head, copied 0.0012 out from it.
- **Kalos's cast** (plan 30 · FM10): every look a person of Kalos stands as (its open areas' and rooms' people, the heroes, the rival, the professor) and every look its trainers' classes stand on the platform in has a twin in `characters.json`'s `grown` block, 81 of them, while `looks` keeps the chibi stand-ins those looks are when asked for plainly; Kalos's file says the grown kit since the user opened the gate on 2026-11-12, so in Kalos the twins are drawn. A twin is its look's colours and parts in a build by age and trade: the heroes, the rival, the four friends, Emma and Essentia, Korrina, Clemont, the idol, picnickers and battle girls `Teen`; youngsters, lasses, bug catchers, school kids, the rich boy and the preschoolers (0.86) `Child`; the old, the veterans, Ramos, Gurkinn and Drasna `GrownElder`; hikers, black belts, sailors, workers, Grant, Wulfric, Wikstrom and the Lumiose gang's men `GrownBroad`; everyone else `Grown`; a height the build doesn't give already eased toward one (Lysandre 1.09, Wulfric 1.06, Sycamore 1.05). The old women are not curved (`curved: false`): their lashes' rule gave them a young figure. Grown touches where a chibi stand-in made do: Valerie's doll's dress is a robe to her ankles, Malva's black suit has its skirt, Lysandre's, Siebold's, Diantha's and Looker's coats are buttoned, Serena carries a bag in her beret's teal. A class shared with the older regions keeps every colour and part of its look, so the same person is known in both kits. Kalos's classes of one person (the professor, the butler, the suspects) are each drawn as the person (`null` in `classes`). `KalosCastGrownTests` holds it.
- **Alola's cast** (the user's switch of 2026-11-12, as Kalos's): the 41 looks Alola's islands, rooms and classes stand as that Kalos's twins didn't cover have twins of their own in the `grown` block, by the same rules: Hau, Lillie, Gladion, the captains (Ilima, Mallow, Lana, Kiawe, Acerola, Sophocles), Hapu and the Skull grunt `Teen`; Hala and the rancher `GrownBroad`; the old scholar and the Battle Tree's keeper `GrownElder` (she not curved); Kukui and his mask, the kahunas, Lusamine, Guzma, Plumeria, Wicke, Dulse, Kahili, Veyra, Oskin and the trades (both swimmers, both breeders, both rangers, the cowgirl, the collector, the dragon tamer, the ruin maniac, the gym guide, the teacher, the middle-aged man and woman) `Grown`. Grown touches: Lillie's and Lusamine's long dresses are robes, Mallow wears her apron, Gladion his jacket, Nanu, Hapu, Veyra and Oskin their coats. `AlolaCastGrownTests` holds it.

#### Live rigs in the field (plan 30 · FM3)

Where the region's kit is grown (`Region.Kit`, `kit` in a region's file; no region's file says so yet, and a tool lays it with `GameEngine.RegionKitOverride`), the field's people are not baked sprites: each is its look **in the grown kit** (`CharacterStyle.InKit(look, Grown)`, decision 4: the player's body follows the region), drawn as the rig itself, smooth and outlined, in the field's light and shadow (decision 1 (c)). Everywhere else the field draws the chibi sprites it always has, to the bit: nothing below is reached where the kit is chibi.

- **Stood up as the field stands everything up.** A live rig is stretched by the field's vertical scale (`1 / cos` of the pitch) as every upright thing is, and the skinned programs straighten it as the scenery's do (`upright`, `pitchSin`, `uprightBase` in the skinned vertex and outline programs), so a rig at the picture's edge stands as straight as a wall beside it (FM0's rigs leaned). The straightening is set on the skinned programs only for the people's own draw and put back to nought straight after (`FieldShaders.SetSkinnedStraightening`), so a battle's, the title's and a studio's rigs never see it. In the mirror (plan 09 · L10) the rigs are straightened about the water's plane and cut off under it, outlines included. The shadow pass draws the body unstretched, as the sun sees it.
- **Twelve live, the rest impostors.** The `FieldRigChoice.MostLive` (12) people nearest the camera's target are live; a person live last frame counts `FieldRigChoice.Stickiness` (1.5 tiles) nearer, so two people at about the same distance never trade places from frame to frame and the swap happens as someone walks out to the crowd's edge. Every live rig keeps its outline's shell and its painted face (the face patch is a fiftieth of a rig's triangles: the plan's "no face beyond six" saved nothing and left blank heads). The rest are **impostors**: the grown rig rendered at the nearest of eight facings (45° each, the field's camera always looking north) standing still into a pixel frame of 48 by 64 at the field's 32 texels to the tile, its smooth face drawn small, outlined in ink by `OutlinePass` and drawn as a card of the people's own program (lit, shadowed, under a bridge's deck); baked once a look, all eight facings in one frame, one look a frame, at most `FieldRigs.MostLooks` (48) looks kept, the least lately used let go.
- **Never waited for.** A grown look is asked for with `TryGet` and drawn once it is made; until then that person is their chibi sprite. The same for an impostor not baked yet. A look whose rig is no person (the briefcase, a rift) is always its sprite.
- **Stances and rides.** Someone standing, sitting on a seat or sitting on the ground is drawn live (plan 30 · FM6: seated as "Scale in a grown region" says); since Kalos's gate (2026-11-12) a **swimmer** treads water live, its feet sunk under the surface by its shoulders' height stretched as it is drawn (`CharacterSprites.WaterLineHeight`), the **player fishing** is their grown look holding the rod, and the **player surfing** kneels live on the Pokémon's field sprite (`Riding.Surf`). Since the gate's owed were shot (`kalosgrown`, 2026-11-12): **someone lying** on a bed lies live on their back along it, on the grown mattress (14/32), their crown 0.3 of a tile behind their tile's middle (the pillow, where the chibi card stands) and their feet toward the way they face (`FieldRigChoice.LieAlong`; someone a script lays down anywhere but a bed lies from their own tile, as the chibi card stays on it); **a fisher of the map** (someone in the fishing or tugging stance) is their grown look holding the rod, as the player fishing is (the rod's look asked for only where the kit is grown); **a ride on land** (the Ride Pager's, Kalos's Mamoswine) is the player astride (`Riding.Mount`, the thighs at the old spread, since a sprite tells no width) on the Pokémon's **grown** field sprite (`FieldSprites.GrownName`), their saddle (`GrownAnimation.SaddleOf`) on the top of its back (`WorldRenderer.GrownRideSeat`, 0.95 of the rows its card fills); the card stands `WorldRenderer.GrownRideAhead` (half a tile) nearer the camera than the rider, past a grown body's depth and its spread knees, so the body astride is wholly behind it and only what rises over its back shows, and the seat is lowered by that step times `tan` of the pitch, as much as the step lowers the card's back on the picture. The rider of a ride on land holds **the reins** (`RideStance.Reins`, the grid's ride alone): the hands down on the ride's back before the hips, under the seat's line, so facing the camera nothing of the hands shows over the card's top. Beyond the live twelve everyone in a stance the kit draws live is an **impostor of their own stance** (`FieldRigs.ImpostorKey`, `HasImpostor`; since 2026-11-12, later still): a sitter baked sitting, a **swimmer** baked treading water (48 by 64, drawn where the live swimmer's feet are sunk, the water hiding what is under it), a **fisher** baked holding the rod (80 by 88, the rod's reach on either side and the tip of a rod held up), and a **lier** baked lying (112 by 64, the body's length on either side), its card stood on the pillow and sunk until the neck is at the mattress's top as the chibi lier's card is, its foot moved along the body so the head lands on the pillow; one look's stance baked a frame, as ever.
- **Under a bridge's deck** a live rig is cut by the deck's boards as a sprite and an impostor are (`Relief.DeckOver`): nothing of the body or its outline is drawn over the deck's tiles above its height (`deckOver` in the character program and the skinned outline's, set for each live figure under a deck and put back to nought after, so no battle or studio sees it).
- **Talking to someone north** (Kalos's gate): a grown body 1.5 tiles tall a tile south of a person covered them to the chest from any of the field's cameras (a tile of ground shows only `sin(pitch)` of a tile on the picture, and the room's close lens flattens the far end of a room more), so while a box of text or a script has the field and the player faces north into someone on the next tile, the player is drawn **half a tile further south** (`WorldRenderer.TalkStepBack`), eased in and out over a quarter second of the camera's clock. Only the picture moves: the player's tile, who they speak to and their motion never do, and nothing of it is drawn where the kit is chibi.
- **The menus' grown portrait**: where the player's look is grown, the Trainer Card's plate and the Pokédex's size page show their impostor facing the eye (`FieldRigs.Portrait`, 48 by 64 texels) in place of the chibi field sprite, and the card's designer and the photo studio frame a grown figure by its own crown (`CharacterStudio.FigureOf`); a chibi look keeps the 1.9 model units round 0.78 it always had.
- **The "!" and the bubbles** stand a grown head over the ground: `FieldRigChoice.BubbleLift` (1.95, a chibi head's, and 0.2 tiles more stretched by the vertical scale, the grown body's extra height), so a bubble clears the hair.
- **A puddle** mirrors each person's impostor of the facing they face, upside down as a sprite is ("Puddles").
- **The light.** The rigs are lit by the field's rig as the scenery is (the skinned program's toon light, the shadow map, fog and cloud shade) and are not graded further: on `fw50`, by day and after dark, they sit in the scene's light beside the impostors; only a look dressed nearly white (a pale coat) glows a little under the bloom, which the looks of plan 30 · FM10 settle.
- **Cost** (counted, never timed on the harness's shared card): a live grown rig is its body (24,000 to 52,000 vertices), its clothes, what it holds, the face patch and two outline shells, five or six draws, and three in the shadow pass; an impostor is two triangles. `FrameProfiler`'s `Characters` lap holds the people's own share of the shadow and scene passes in a grown place; the harness's `freewalk` shot `fw50` and `profile`'s `freewalk_crowd` scene print the live and impostor counts, the triangles and the lap.

**Grown animation** (plan 30 · FM5; `Graphics/GrownAnimation.cs`, `Graphics/GrownMotion.cs`, GPU-free). A rig of the grown kit is posed by clips of its own; a chibi rig is posed by the chibi clips exactly as before (`CharacterAnimation.Apply` hands a grown humanoid to `GrownAnimation.Apply` and nothing else changes; the chibi clips stay `CharacterAnimation.ApplyChibi`, which a tool may still ask of a grown body). The grown clips read the same `CharacterPose` and a few fields of their own, which a chibi rig never reads (`RunBlend`, `Stride`, `Lean`, `StepTurn` and `StepTurnAngle`, `Start`, `Stop`, `Talking`, `Riding`, `Skating`, `Gliding`).

- **The legs stand on the ground.** Wherever a grown body stands, walks or runs, its legs are solved to where its feet should be (two bones by the knee's bend, then the thigh turned onto the target, the foot set flat to the ground under whatever the root and the hips do, the toes flat when the heel lifts): a dip of the root, a bow from the hips, a lean or a breath never sinks a foot into the ground or lifts it off. Only a hop, a stance and a ride set the legs by hand.
- **The walk and the run are one gait**, blended by `RunBlend` (0 walking, 1 running), its cycle advanced by the distance walked over the stride (`Walk += distance / Stride`; two steps a cycle), so a foot never slides at any speed. Each foot is on the ground for a share of the cycle (0.58 walking, 0.38 running), and while it is down its ball moves straight back under the body at the body's own speed, half the stride's share either side of its rest (so a stride of `S` carries the ball `S × share` over the ground, which is the distance the body goes meanwhile); the heel lifts over the stance's last third (0.55 rad walking, 0.7 running) about the ball, which stays put, and the toes bend back flat; in the air the foot comes forward on a curve that leaves the ground and meets it again at the ground's own speed under the body (so it is still in the world as it lifts and as it lands, and swings on a little behind first), lifted 0.10 of the leg walking and 0.26 running at the middle, toes up a little before it lands flat. The root drops where it must so the stance leg reaches (no leg longer than 0.995 of its two bones walking, a knee bent about 11°, and 0.95 running; standing, 0.9985, about 6°), so the hips sink as the legs spread; running, it rises off the ground between steps by 0.03 of the leg. Arms swing against the legs (0.28 walking, 0.55 running at the shoulder), the elbows bend from 0.2 to 1.35 as the run comes in, the chest turns against the hips (0.07), the body leans forward from 0.04 to 0.2 and the head looks ahead. The stride is 0.75 to 1.4 of the leg (hip joint to ankle) from standing to walking pace, then grows to 2.4 at twice walking pace and no further; walking pace is 1.75 legs a second, and the run comes in between 1.2 and 1.8 times it (`GrownMotion.WalkPace`, `RunFrom`, `RunTo`). Standing still, `WalkBlend` is 0; it eases in with the speed to a third of walking pace.
- **What follows through.** A coat's tails (`CoatTail`) swing back with the stride and spread with the legs apart (a sixth wider at a full stride), so the legs come forward in front of them; a skirt flares with the stride (0.42 deeper at a full walking stride, 0.62 running, an eighth to a sixth wider) and, running, tips forward up to 0.45 and flares 0.4 deeper and 0.08 wider as the foot in the air rises toward a third of the leg, so a knee coming up stays under it (plan 30 · FM10; with the skirt's share of each thigh no thigh stands out of it by more than half an outline, a few millimetres, anywhere in a walk or a run); the hair and the bag bounce a beat behind as the chibi kit's do; the collarbones rise and fall a little with the arms; the toes bend at every push off.
- **Turning** (`GrownMotion`): the body's yaw eases toward the way it goes at 18 a second standing or walking and 7 running (a run curves); from standing, a turn of more than 120° is a **step-turn** of 0.25 s, the yaw carried round on a smooth step while the head and then the chest lead it, one foot lifted and set down and then the other. Moving, the body **leans into a turn** by its sideways acceleration against gravity (9.2 model units a second squared), at most 8°, eased in over a tenth of a second; the feet stay flat under the lean and the head turns a little into the curve.
- **Starts and stops** are under 0.2 s each (`GrownMotion.StartSeconds`, `StopSeconds`, 0.18): starting, the weight shifts forward and the hips dip into the first step; stopping, the body settles back over the feet, the hips dip and the arms swing forward once. Neither holds the body back, so the grid's quickness is kept.
- **The emotes** are the chibi kit's names and lengths (`CharacterAnimation.Duration`, so a script's timings and a throw's release at 0.3 s hold), made for a grown body: the **wave** is the right hand above the shoulder, waving from the elbow; **surprised** a start back with the hands up before the chest; the **nod** split between the neck and the head; the **cheer** both arms up in a V, the body bouncing on its knees; the **throw** overarm with the chest turning into it and the collarbone carrying the shoulder back and over; the **bow** from the hips, the spine and the head following and the arms hanging straight down; the **shrug** both collarbones up and the forearms out, palms up; the **point** the right arm straight ahead at the shoulder's height, the chest turned after it; the **laugh** the head back and the chest shaking, the hands at the belly. Each starts and ends at the standing pose, over the same envelope as the chibi kit's.
- **Talking** (`Talking`): the right forearm raised before the body with the hand open, beating gently with the words, the head nodding a little: what a trainer who speaks (plan 11 · C13) and anyone in conversation does.
- **Stances** at a grown body's size. **Sitting**, the seat's top is 0.45 of the hip joints' height (`GrownAnimation.SeatShare`; a chair in a grown region is 12/32 of a tile high, a bench or a sofa 11/32, a cushion 3/32, a bed 14/32: `Seats.GrownHeightOf`), the hip joints a little over it, the pelvis tilted back 0.3 and the spine forward to keep the back straight (so the thigh turns about 70° at the hip, not a quarter turn, and the hip's crease doesn't tear), the feet on the floor below the knees, the hands on the lap, a skirt and a coat's tails lying forward. **On the ground** the legs lie out ahead, the toes up; **lying**, the body lies on its back as the chibi kit's does, laid along a bed with its feet toward the way the lier faces ("Live rigs in the field"). **Swimming** is treading water upright with long breaststroke sweeps; **fishing** and **tugging** are the chibi kit's with a grown reach, a foot a little ahead; since 2026-11-12 (later still) the rod is held **up**: the hands at the chest a little to the right of the body, the right forearm bent past level (one level toward the eye puts the hand at the waist on the picture), the wrist turned little to the side, and the rod rises some 70° over the water, steeper than the field's camera looks down (59°), so that seen from the front it rises on the picture too (held lower, a rod toward the eye ran down the picture and read as held low). **Riding** (`Riding`): on a **Surf**er's back kneeling, the shins along the back behind, the body forward over the right hand held ahead and the left on the thigh; on a **Bicycle** seated with the hands forward on the bars and the feet on a crank turning with the cycle; on a **Mount** astride, the thighs out round its back and the hands forward, crouching lower as it runs (`RunBlend`); since plan 30 · FM13 the thighs spread to clear the mount's own half width at the seat with a thigh's girth to spare (`Astride`, a roll of 0.34 to 0.6 at the hip, past which the crotch's weights stretch), and the stance follows what the mount does (`RideStance`): **dash** the spine and neck further over the hands and the coat's tails up, **swim** kneeling on its back as on a Surfer's (its floats are too wide to sit astride), **glide** sitting up with the arms out wide and the coat and hair blown back, **climb** leant 0.6 forward about the saddle onto its back, the thighs turned back by as much, with the head turned up the face (open world, "The mount"), and **reins** (the grid's ride on land alone) the arms down and the hands on the ride's back before the hips.
- **Skating** (`Skating`, Kalos's roller skates, the gate's owed): the gait's place is a skater's stride, one push for each two of the gait's cycles: each foot in turn driven out to its own side (0.26 of the leg) and a little back (0.16) on its wheels for 0.42 of its cycle (`GrownAnimation.SkatePush`), lifted 0.1 of the leg and brought home under the body by 0.58 (`SkateReturn`), then rolling there while the other pushes; the hips dropped 0.07 of the leg (`SkateCrouch`) and swaying over the pushing foot, the spine forward 0.24 with the head up to look ahead, the hips turned toward the push and the chest against them, the arms swinging across the body against the pushing leg, the coat and the hair streaming back. **Gliding** (`Gliding`: skates let go of, or ice) stands on both feet with the knees a little bent, leaning forward and the arms out for balance.
- **Hopping** (a ledge, a jump on the spot): a crouch into the hop, the knees tucked at its top with the arms out at the shoulders for balance, and the legs reaching for the ground as it ends.

#### Scale in a grown region (plan 30 · FM6)

Where the region's kit is grown (`Region.Kit`; Galar's, Kalos's and, since 2026-11-12, Alola's files say so, and a tool lays it with `GameEngine.RegionKitOverride`), the world round the people is sized to a grown body. A chibi region keeps every number it had: nothing of this section reaches Sinnoh to Unova while their files say `Chibi`.

- **Buildings and props for the Kalos map's sessions** (plan 30, decision 2; a grown adult 1.6 tiles tall, a teen 1.5; heights in tiles before the field's vertical stretch, the art 32 texels to the tile as everywhere):

  | Piece | Size | Note |
  |---|---|---|
  | A door | 1 tile wide, 1.75 tall (56 texels) | the opening; a lintel or a sign above it, never in it. A grown adult's hair clears it by a twentieth of a tile |
  | A shop's counter | 0.55 tall (18 texels) | a clerk's hands rest on it at the waist; spoken to across it as anywhere |
  | A chair's seat | 12/32 | `Seats.GrownHeightOf`: 0.45 of a grown adult's hip joints, the feet on the floor |
