- **The Galactic mark** (plan 09 · L13, decision 6; `GalacticMark`): Team Galactic's mark as this game draws it, which is not the original's. A lozenge (a square stood on its corner) cut like a stone into six facets round a dark core, with seams from the core to its top and bottom points and to the middle of each of its four sides. The facets are flat shades of their buildings' yellow band, lit from above: the upper two `248,228,128`, the two at the sides `232,204,76`, the lower two `196,164,48`. The seams and the rim are ink (`52,44,62`, or the dark of whatever it lies on), the core a lozenge a third as wide in the same ink, with a spark of the slit windows' teal `120,230,220` at its heart. It is one description (its facets as shapes, and a painter that lays the same shapes down in whole texels), so it is the same mark wherever it is shown: thrown at the picture as a grunt's or a Commander's battle begins, on the grunts' uniforms (plan 11 · C2), and over the doors of their buildings, where at fourteen texels only the lozenge and its core are left (the seams come in at 24 texels across, the spark at 48).
- **Scene effects** (plan 09 · L11; `SceneFx`): what a story's scene lights on a tile with a line of its script. Each is pixel art in cells of 32 texels (a tile), in whole texels and a few frames, and fades in the same two steps of alpha as everything here (170, then 90) over its last stretch; each plays its own sound as it begins. Eleven kinds (the last five plan 29 · LS10's, asked for by the legendary scenes' shots):

| Kind | What is drawn | Frames | Colours | Length (going) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Burst | light thrown out of a point half a tile above the ground: a core with four rays (14 texels across), then eight (24), then eight broken and thinning (30), a tenth of a second each; eight sparks fly out on the screen's plane to 40 texels in half a second, slowing, those thrown downward kept low, each a star of 7 texels, then a cross of 5, then a dot of 2 that lingers to the end | 3 and 3 | core and rays `255,244,208`; sparks `255,246,196` | 0.6 s (the last 0.3) |
| Beam | a column standing on the tile, four tiles tall and two more fading (170, then 90), with a glow lying round its foot (28 by 14 texels, half alpha) and two motes of 2 texels rising up it; it grows from 12 texels wide to 18 a texel each side at a time over 0.2 s, breathes between 16 and 18 ten times a second, and narrows the same way as it goes; its edges step in and out a texel every four rows | 4 | core `248,252,255`, edges `206,232,255`, its outermost texel `150,196,240` | 1.5 s (0.2) |
| Rift | a tear standing in the air, a tile wide and two tall, its foot half a tile up: a slit for 0.12 s, half open until 0.25 s, then open, its ragged rim crawling through four frames at eight a second, shedding a mote of its rim to each side; it closes the same way | 6 | rim `255,214,255` and `214,110,246`, inside `30,14,48` with specks `90,52,128` | 3 s (0.25) |
| Shadow | a dark shape with wings spread lying on the ground, three tiles by two, crossing sixteen tiles the way it is sent, from eight before its tile to eight beyond, in whole texels; its wings beat in two frames, spread and drawn in, twice a second | 2 | `20,14,36` at alpha 120, its rim at 60 | 2 s (0.25) |
| Ripple | the whole picture's rows pushed sideways by whole texels of the field (a texel is three pixels at 1080p, a row 1/360 of the height) on a sine 24 rows long that travels two waves a second, stepped thirty times a second; it reaches one texel, then two, then three a tenth of a second apart, and steps back down | — | — | 1.2 s (0.3) |
| Sparkles | eight points of light rising round the tile, within three quarters of a tile either side and from a tenth of a tile to a tile and a tenth up, each a star of 7 texels, a cross of 5, a dot of 2 and gone, over 0.5 s of a cycle of 0.6 s begun at a moment of its own, rising a texel every sixteenth of a second | 3 | `255,246,196` | 1.5 s (0.5) |
| Geyser | a column of water two tiles wide heaving up out of the tile in front of whatever lies in it: to three tiles tall in a third of a second, a tile at a time with a band of foam on top (the crown, painted in its own colours, the column's top under it), standing until a second, falling back in the same steps by 1.3 s; its streaks crawl up two frames at eight a second, and a ring of foam spreads on the ground under it from one tile across to three; as it falls ten drops are thrown out of its crown, each a star, a cross and a dot, arcing out and down to the ground | 2 and 2 | water `110,180,240` (its streaks and rim lighter by the grey painted under them), foam and drops `230,244,255` | 1.6 s (0.3) |
| Eruption | the geyser's shape and steps in magma: the column `240,100,40`, its crown and the embers it throws `255,236,140`, its ring `240,100,40` | 2 and 2 | as said | 1.6 s (0.3) |
| Stair | a stair of light climbing away north and up from the tile: twelve steps, each 28 texels wide, a tread of white light four texels deep over a riser of the beam's blue (16 texels in all), standing a quarter of a tile further north and a quarter higher than the one before so they stand on one another, lit one after another a tenth of a second apart with a spark blinking on each as it comes; held, it stands whole; the glow of the beam lies round its foot, and two motes climb it, a step every tenth of a second | 1 | tread `248,252,255`, riser `150,196,240`, its rim and lowest rows `104,150,220` | 3 s (0.4) |
| Petals | ten petals blown up and round the tile, from a sixth of a tile up, each a lozenge of seven texels by five broad side on and a sliver of six by two edge on, turning four times a second, rising a texel every twelfth of a second and drifting out to a tile and a half either side, each round of 0.9 s begun at a moment of its own | 2 | pink `255,170,205`, every third paler `255,214,230`, their edges deeper by the grey painted under them | 1.5 s (0.4) |
| Mist | a dark mist lying low round the tile: seven puffs a tile across, flat on the ground, creeping out from the tile to about a tile away, close enough to run together, over the effect's length and wavering a texel twice a second; it gathers in the two steps of alpha (90, 170) over its first 0.3 s as it goes out over its last | 2 | `24,16,40` at alpha 110, its rim at half that | 3 s (0.5) |

A burst whitens the whole picture over the composite, as lightning does: 85 % white for its first twentieth of a second, then 50 %, then 20 % until a fifth of a second, then nothing. The effects are light and are drawn undimmed at every hour; only the shadow and the mist, which are dark, are drawn as they are. A geyser or an eruption stands in front of whoever is on its tile, so the Pokémon in the pool is hidden while the column stands and is there again as it falls: that is the Pokémon rising. A held effect (a beam on a machine) plays until it is stopped, a held burst or shadow over and over, and a stopped one goes as it would at its end; coming to another place clears them all.

- **Set pieces** (plan 09 · L12; `SetPieces`): what a scene does to someone standing in it, and a lift's ride. Each is a function of the time since its script's line, stepped thirty times a second in whole texels, and the script waits for it; where they stand, and who can be spoken to, never changes.
  - *A shake*: whoever is shaken is drawn three texels east of their tile, back on it, three texels west and back, a thirtieth of a second each, so many times over (Regigigas eight times as it wakes, a little over a second). Their shadow goes with them.
  - *A flicker*: whoever flickers is not drawn for a fifth of a second, then drawn for a fifth, changing so many times in all (Cresselia six: gone, back, gone, back, gone, back, and then it is taken off the map). Nothing fades: they are there or they are not.
  - *A lift's shudder*: while a car is between floors the whole picture is thrown a texel up for a thirtieth of a second in every four, and as it stops two texels down for a fifteenth. It is the car that moves, so nothing in it moves against anything else.
- **Festivals** (plan 40 · GE9; `FestivalArt`, `Data/festivals.json`): on a festival's days its town is dressed with things of the map shown by `FLAG_FESTIVAL_<ID>`, each a card of our own (the scenery's 32 texels a tile, whole texels, the one-texel outline every card has), standing at its tile's middle and in the way like a person on that tile alone (the strung ones are two tiles wide and overhang their neighbours), on open ground off the paths, doorways and signs. They are drawn as cards of a wall (`MakeCard(..., partOfAWall: true)`, as an open door is), so what is marked lit (`ArtSheet.PublicLight`, only the core of a lantern, a bulb or a star, never its rim) turns the rig's lamp colour after dark and burns all night; by day it is the paper's own colour. Nothing of them is white, and nothing is taken from any game.
- **Gathering spots** (plan 39 · CR2; `GatherArt`, `materials.json`'s `gather`): things of the map an overlay stands on a route (`npcType` `GatherRock`, `GatherWood`, `GatherPlant`, `GatherShore`, `GatherSnow`), shown only once the crafting kit is in the bag (`FLAG_GOT_CRAFTING_KIT`), each a card of 30 by 26 texels at its tile's middle with the one-texel outline every card has, in the way like a person on that tile alone and spoken to from any side. Each kind has two looks, swapped as the game tells the spot whether it bears today: **a rock** is a grey boulder (`132,128,136`) with a smaller one at its foot, bearing loose stones round it and a glint of ore in its face (`214,170,78`, its lit texel `250,222,140`); **a fallen log** is a short trunk on its side (bark `112,78,52`, its cut end pale `214,176,120`), bearing chips of wood round it, a bead of amber resin on the bark and a twig with a leaf; **a herb bush** is a low round bush of three greens, bearing four sprigs standing out of it with pale flowers; **a heap on the shore** is a low mound of sand (`214,196,150`), bearing a pink fan shell, a pale spiral shell and a crust of salt; **a drift of snow** is a soft mound kept well below white (`206,216,232`), bearing two crystals of ice (`150,200,232`, their spines `206,236,248`). Bare, each is the same without what is loose on it, so a spot picked today is still there to see. They stand on open ground of their own area with every tile round them open, off the paths, doorways, signs and triggers; a shore's within four tiles of water and a snow drift's on snow. Nothing of them is taken from any game.
  - *Paper lanterns on a string* (64 by 54): two dark wooden poles `96,64,44` (lit `128,90,62`) three texels thick at the card's edges and a cord `70,52,40` sagging between them five texels at its middle, with five round lanterns hung from it, red `214,72,56` and orange `236,140,52` in turn, each 8 by 10 with a darker band at top, foot and right side and a core of 3 by 5 marked lit. Hearthome City's Night of Lanterns, Unova's Bridgelight in Castelia's plaza and Sunyshore's Summer Night.
  - *The moon lantern* (32 by 64): Kanto's Harvest Moon in Celadon City: one pole with a crook at its top from which hangs a round lantern 22 across of pale gold `246,214,120` (shaded `214,170,80`) with a crescent of shade at its right, a red cap and tassel; its middle 11 by 12 is marked lit.
  - *A lit pine* (36 by 64): Snowpoint's Midwinter Lights: a pine of three tiers in the field's dark green `40,96,64` (lit `64,128,80`), snow along each tier's edges and foot `214,226,244`, a trunk `96,64,44`, and twelve bulbs of 2 by 2 in warm yellow `250,210,110` and red `230,90,80` round its tiers, each marked lit.
  - *A blossom arch* (56 by 58): Floaroma's Blossom Days: two posts wound in green `86,150,70` holding a round arch of blossom two rings deep, pink `240,160,190` and white-pink `250,214,226` clusters of 5 by 5 with gold and deep pink hearts, a few leaves among them and a few petals `236,130,170` fallen at its feet. Nothing lit.
  - *A stall's awning* (40 by 44): every town festival's stall, beside its keeper: a counter of planks `164,112,72` (top `196,146,96`) under a striped awning of eight stripes, each five texels, red `206,64,56` and cream `236,226,196` in turn, the same for every festival, a hem cut in steps a texel deep, and two jars and a basket of fruit on the counter. Nothing lit.
  - *Bells and a maple garland* (52 by 54): Johto's Bellfall in Ecruteak: a red-lacquered frame `176,52,44` (lit `206,80,62`) like a small gate, its beam hung with three bronze bells `176,130,58` (lit `214,170,84`), the middle one larger, and a garland of maple leaves along the beam in red `206,72,48`, orange `226,128,52` and gold `220,176,70`, a few fallen at its feet. Nothing lit.
  - *Shell and flag bunting* (64 by 52): Hoenn's Tide Day in Slateport: two white-blue poles `200,214,230` with a line between them carrying nine triangular flags of six texels, sea blue `70,140,210`, sand `232,206,150` and coral `236,120,100` in turn, and a scallop shell `246,214,190` at each pole's top. Nothing lit.
  - *A music stand* (36 by 56): Kalos's Solstice Serenade in Lumiose City's two parks south of the Prism Tower, beside the paper lanterns: a stand of wrought iron `52,56,70` (lit `92,98,118`), a tripod of three feet splayed nine texels from the rod's foot, a rod two texels thick, a desk holding a sheet of songs in cream `236,226,196` (shaded `206,192,158`) leaning back a texel every eight rows, on it two staves of three lines with five notes each in ink `70,52,40`, a scroll of iron arching five texels over the desk and curling in at its corners, a small round lamp of pale gold `246,214,120` at the scroll's top (its middle 2 by 2 marked lit), and a bow of violet `150,96,196` (deep `104,60,148`) and gold `220,176,70` tied round the rod with a tail of each hanging. It is Kalos's own: an iron scroll in the city's style, never a lacquered gate or a paper string.
  - *A lei arch* (64 by 54): Alola's Garland Days in Hau'oli City's plaza: two poles of bamboo `176,168,92` (lit `214,206,130`) with a darker knot `132,124,66` every eight rows, a lei of flowers swung between their tops sagging six texels at its middle, each flower five texels across (a 3 by 3 middle and a petal out on each side), one every five texels, in hibiscus pink `236,110,150`, plumeria cream `246,236,200` with a yellow heart `246,206,90`, and orange `240,150,70` in turn, two leaves `72,150,80` between every two flowers; a shorter lei hangs down each pole to half its height, and on each pole's top a crystal of four by six in pale aqua `150,220,220` (deep `90,170,180`) for the Z-Move poses danced under it, its middle 2 by 3 marked lit. It is Alola's own: bamboo, flowers and a crystal, never paper lanterns or a lacquered frame.
  - *Hay bales and a rosette* (44 by 36): Galar's Wool Fair, inside Wedgehurst's Pokémon Center: two round bales of straw `214,184,104` (shade `176,146,72`, straws in rows of `236,212,140` every third row) stacked one on two, the top one smaller, a red rosette `200,52,56` with gold tails `220,176,70` pinned to the top bale's face, and a tuft of fleece `236,232,222` (shade `200,196,186`) heaped on the bale's top for the fair's shearing. Nothing lit. It is Galar's own: straw and a prize rosette, never bunting or a stall's awning.
  - *Sheaves of grain* (36 by 56): Hisui's Sheaf Feast on Jubilife Village's training ground: three sheaves of wheat `220,184,96` (stalks `176,140,66`, ears `236,206,120` in a fringe at the top) leaning together, bound at their waists with a band of straw rope `150,110,62`, and a stake of plain wood `120,84,56` behind them carrying a strip of paper `236,226,196` with three red marks `190,56,48` down it, the Galaxy Team's tally of the first harvest. Nothing lit. It is Hisui's own: bound grain and a paper tally, never a painted egg or a lantern.
  - *The holidays* (`FLAG_FESTIVAL_CHRISTMAS`, `NEW_YEAR`, `EASTER`, `VALENTINE`), in each region's festival town (Sinnoh's in Jubilife City's park, which is the region's biggest town and hosts none of the four; Kalos's in Lumiose City, Alola's in Hau'oli City, Hisui's in Jubilife Village, the hub's in Wayhaven and Galar's inside Wedgehurst's Pokémon Center): **a decorated pine** (40 by 72), the lit pine's tree with a gold star `246,214,90` at its top (its middle marked lit), round baubles of 3 by 3 in red, blue `90,130,220` and gold and a ribbon of red winding down it; **a basket of painted eggs** (20 by 16), a woven basket `176,124,70` (its weave in a darker row every other texel) heaped with six eggs of 4 by 5 in pastel pink `240,180,200`, blue `170,200,240`, yellow `246,226,140` and green `180,220,160`, each with a stripe or dots of a deeper shade; **a New Year's banner** (56 by 50), a cloth of deep red `176,52,44` 48 by 15 hung between two poles with a gold border `220,176,70` and four gold squares for its words, two small round lanterns hung from its ends with their cores lit; and for St Valentine's, something quiet: **a heart on a post** (14 by 26), a short white-painted post `226,222,212` tied with a pink ribbon `236,140,170` whose bow hangs a texel to each side, and a small red heart card `214,72,90` of 6 by 5 pinned at its top. Nothing of the holidays is lit but the star and the banner's lanterns.
- **The Summer Night's bursts** (plan 40 · GE9): in Sunyshore City after dark on the festival's days, coming to the city lights the scene effects' `burst` (plan 09 · L11, its white flash and its sound) over the water east of the Poké Mart, and `sparkles` beside it: once, as the city is come to, from its own `OnEnter`.

### Legendary scenes

Plan 29. Every legendary or mythical Pokémon met in a place of its own is met through a short staged beat before its battle, written in the place's own script with the camera kit, the scene effects and the set pieces above, and nothing else: no picture or sound made outside the engine. `LegendaryScenesTests.EveryLegendaryBattleOfAPlaceHasItsBeat` holds it: before every `wildbattle` of a species whose data says `Legendary` or `Mythical` stands a `scene` of the same script with a camera line that looks somewhere (`pan`, `zoom`, `path` or `follow`) and a sign (`effect`, `shake`, `flicker` or `anim`), but for the meetings its `StillBare` list names with the session that owes them.

**The five beats.** Six to nine seconds in all, never more than ten once the cry and the line are read:

| Beat | Time | What it is |
|---|---|---|
| 1. Hush | 0.5 s | `scene "<Name> at <place>"`, `music stop`, the player faced to the Pokémon, `letterbox on 0.4`. A question the original asks first ("Will you challenge…?") is asked before the scene, so a no leaves nothing to skip. |
| 2. Look | 1 to 2 s | `camera pan` to the Pokémon's tile (`camera path` for one met high above or far off), then `camera zoom 1.4 0.8`. A room is seen whole: the zoom alone, the pan moving only within its slack. |
| 3. Sign | 1 to 3 s | one scene effect by the Pokémon's kind (the table below), on its tile or between it and the player, and a set piece on the Pokémon where it fits. |
| 4. Voice | 1 to 1.5 s | `cry` with `camera shake` where the Pokémon is loud, an `emote player exclaim` where the player would start, and one line of our own (two at most) saying what the player sees; never the games' line. |
| 5. Hand over | 0.7 s | a held effect stopped, `camera release 0.4`, `letterbox off 0.3`, `endscene`, then `wildbattle` exactly as before. The release comes before the battle, so plan 09 · L14's way in widens from the place's own lens. |

| Kind of Pokémon | Effect | Set piece and camera |
|---|---|---|
| Time, space, the other world (Dialga, Palkia, Giratina, Darkrai) | `rift` held, `ripple` (a shadow for Giratina, which is met in its own form; Darkrai's a dream's `mist` held round it, not a rift) | `slowmo 0.5 1.5` |
| Fire, lava, the sun (Moltres, Ho-Oh, Heatran, Groudon, Reshiram) | `burst`, then `ripple` (heat); one lying in magma rises out of it in an `eruption` | `camera shake 0.6` |
| Lightning (Zapdos, Raikou, Thundurus, Zekrom) | two `burst`s 0.4 s apart | `camera shake 0.4` on each |
| Ice, sea, rain (Articuno, Lugia, Kyogre, Suicune, Kyurem, Regice) | `sparkles`, `ripple`; one lying in water rises out of it in a `geyser` | `camera shake 0.5` for the sea's ones |
| Stone and steel giants (the Regis, Regigigas, Terrakion, Cobalion) | `beam` on the statue's tile, its eyes lighting | `shake <who> 8`, `camera shake 0.8` |
| Mind, dreams, the lakes (Mewtwo, Mew, Uxie, Mesprit, Azelf, Cresselia, Latias, Latios, Deoxys, Jirachi) | `sparkles`, `ripple` | `flicker <who>` for one that is there and not there |
| Flight overhead (Rayquaza, Lugia, Ho-Oh, the forces of nature) | `shadow` crossing the ground toward the player | `camera path` up the shadow's way |
| Grass and woods (Virizion, Celebi, Shaymin) | `sparkles` (`petals` for Shaymin) | `camera follow <who>` |
| The one who made the world (Arceus) | a `stair` of light up to where it waits, held while the way is climbed; `burst` and `ripple` where it stands | `slowmo 0.5 1.5` |
| Ghost in a machine (Rotom) | `sparkles` on the set's tile | the set's own face: a flicker or a sound of the set |

Rules: the scene ends before the battle, so the hold skips the beat and never the battle (plan 12 · Q20); what the scene leaves (flags, variables, `hide`, `show`, the battle and its options) is what the bare script left, line for line; effects only on the Pokémon's tile and the tiles between it and the player, never through a wall; a roamer's grass meeting is L14's alone, and only the scene that sets it roaming is staged. A beat the kit can't write is asked of plan 29 · LS10 as a new effect kind, here first.

### Light, shadow and grading (day)

| Setting | Value |
|---|---|
| Sun direction | normalize(−0.6, 0.8, 0.42) |
| Sun colour | 0.66, 0.55, 0.40 (golden) |
| Sky / ground ambient | 0.50, 0.55, 0.74 / 0.46, 0.44, 0.40 |
| Diffuse | smooth Lambert (the pixel art carries the shading) |
| Shadows | soft and without grain: nine lookups of the shadow map on High, one texel apart, each the graphics card's own filtered comparison of the four texels round it, so that an edge is one even ramp four texels wide (about a dozen pixels at 4K). Four lookups on Medium, one on Low. Lookups further apart than a texel leave steps in the edge. On a machine where the card can't be asked to compare, a Poisson disc 1.8 texels in radius, rotated per pixel, stands in |
| Cloud shade | 20 % dimmer sunlight in broad patches that drift slowly across the map |
| Fog | toward `0.72, 0.82, 0.92`, 18 % at the far edge (starts 44 units from the camera, full at 72) |
| Ambient occlusion | screen-space, quarter resolution, read from a half-resolution copy of the depth buffer, radius 0.45 units, strength 0.4: grounds walls, props and sprites |
| Tilt-shift | a blur growing to 6 texels in radius (a disc of 12 taps; within 2.5 texels five taps are as dense as the texels and do), plus 95 % mix toward a wide blur outside a ±30 % focus band |
| Bloom | threshold 0.86, strength 0.38 |
| Grade | saturation 1.10, contrast 1.08, shadows × (0.88, 0.92, 1.10), highlights × (1.07, 1.00, 0.90) |
| Vignette | 0.26 |
| Rooms | their own warm lamp light at every hour; tilt-shift 4, depth of field 60 %, focus ±36 %, bloom threshold 0.93 at 0.22 |

The camera target and every sprite snap to the texel grid (1/32 unit), so pixel art scrolls in whole texels and doesn't shimmer. The other times of day are under *Time of day* below.

**Photo mode's filters** (plan 12 · Q16, `Graphics/PhotoFilters.cs`) are laid over the grade above, last, after the weather, the climate and the season, and only while a photo is being composed: *Vivid* is saturation × 1.25 and contrast × 1.06; *Mono* is saturation 0, both tints back to 1 and contrast × 1.10; *Sepia* is saturation 0.3 with shadows × (1.14, 0.94, 0.68) and highlights × (1.24, 1.00, 0.68) (the composite tints before it takes the colour out, so sepia keeps a little colour for its tints to show in); *Soft* is bloom strength × 1.6 at a threshold 0.08 lower, contrast × 0.94 and vignette + 0.12. A borrowed hour is the middle of its period (`GameClock`'s own: 7, 13.5, 18.5, 22, 2) and goes through the same rig as the clock's; a borrowed weather is drawn and lit as the place's own would be, outdoors only. Neither touches the clock, the date or the place's weather.

### Soaring

Plan 38 · EX7. Once a region's Hall of Fame is set, Fly's choice offers SOAR: the team's flier carries the player over the region. Everything here is our own; no game's soaring screen, its sky or its beacons is taken.

- **The region from the sky** is the region's own picture (`RegionMapPictures`, a texel a tile, the Town Map's), laid flat under the sky at the height of the ground and seen from behind and above the flier through a camera that keeps its distance and its pitch: 34° under the horizon at the lowest height, 52° at the highest, the five heights between eased, never a jump. Nothing of the picture is redrawn: its texels stay square and are filtered by nothing, so the land reads as the Town Map's, from above. Past the picture's edge is the sea's deep blue (40, 72, 128) to the horizon.
- **The light is the hour's** (`ArtLook`'s outdoor rig at the clock's hour): the sun's colour on the land, the sky's two colours above it, fog the sky's lower colour from three quarters of the way to the horizon. After dark the land is dimmed as the field is and the beacons are what is brightest.
- **Clouds** are flat sheets of our own over the land, between the second and the fourth height, soft-edged white (244, 246, 250) at two thirds, their shadows on the land the cloud's shape in (0, 0, 0) at a sixth; the flier passes through a sheet, which then fills the picture at a third for as long as the flier is in it. They drift with the wind of the hour (`Weathers.Wind`), never with the camera.
- **A beacon** stands over every place Fly lands that is open: a column of light one cell wide from the land to the top of the sky, in the soft gold of the region map's open places (255, 214, 120), brightest at its foot and thinning upward, with a ring on the land at its foot that pulses once a second. A place not yet open has no beacon. The beacon under the flier (within three quarters of a cell of its foot) turns white and its place's name stands over it in a pill, as the Town Map names a hovered place, with CONFIRM to land.
- **The rider** is the flier's battle model seen from behind, wings at their beat (its Fly clip where a model has one, its idle otherwise), with the player's own look in the field sitting on its back, the two banked together into a turn by up to 20°. A flier lent by the field helpers (`FieldHelp`) is drawn the same.
- **The interface** is the field's: the place's name in a pill at the top while over it, the height as five pips at the right, and a hint row (TURN, CLIMB, DIVE, FASTER, LAND, BACK) at the foot. Nothing else is drawn over the sky.
- **Don't**: draw the land at a scale of its own, filter its texels, let a cloud hide a beacon's foot (a beacon is drawn after the clouds), or show a beacon for a shut place.

## Battles (3D)

### Models

- Pokémon and trainers are real 3D models on the platforms (no pixelation), lit and shadowed with the stage. Trainers are the SDF characters of the field, skinned on the GPU, with their smooth faces.
- Size rule: a Pokémon covers the part of the screen its 128-px sprite frame would, i.e. the frame spans the platform's width ÷ 158 px (opponent) or ÷ 150 px (player).
- Shading: two-tone cel ramp with a soft terminator (`smoothstep(0, 0.12, N·L)`), hemisphere ambient × 1.08, rim light 0.30.
- Outlines: offset shells of the SDF (the surface pushed out, meshed on its own and skinned with the body, drawn with front faces culled), so creases, brims and gaps between limbs never show spots of ink; colour = surface mixed toward `34,26,40`. A character's shell is 0.009 units out; a Pokémon's is half a pixel of its 128-px sprite frame (`WorldPerPixel × 0.55`).
- Flashes (send-out, hit, recall, the dark silhouette of a wild Pokémon before the camera settles) are colour blends in the shader, not extra sprites.

### Pokémon (version 2, G7)

- **One smooth body** sculpted with the SDF kit (`PokeBuilder` on `SdfModel`): ellipsoids, round cones, flattened spikes, rings and boxes joined with small smooth blends (2.5 cm for body parts, 1–1.5 cm for spikes and claws, sharp for a jaw's lip), meshed at 128 cells across the model's largest dimension (17,000–47,000 vertices) and cached on disk. Colour regions that shouldn't change the shape (masks, bands, a flame's heart) are paint with a soft edge 1.4 cm wide, or they follow the mesh's vertices in steps.
- **Materials**: fur and feathers matt (`Fur`), smooth hides with a small sheen (`Scales`), shells, claws, beaks, teeth and horns hard and glossy (`Shell`), gold and steel `Metal`, leaves `Leaf`, flames `Glow`.
- **Body plans** set the skeleton and how the clips move it: biped, quadruped, bird, serpent, fish, floating. Every plan has a root at the feet and a body bone at the middle of the torso; legs hang from the root so they stay planted while the body breathes above them; head, jaw, arms, wings, tail, ears, leaves, flames and fins have bones of their own; a serpent is a chain of segments.
- **Eyes and markings are decals**, never sculpted: each is a 96-px square of an atlas laid over the triangles under it (projected along its normal, lifted a hair off the surface) and cut out where it is empty. The eye or marking fills the middle half of its square, so coarse triangles never clip it. Eyes come in five states, each painted with anti-aliased shapes: **open** (a dark oval whose lower part is the iris, lighter toward the bottom, with a big glint upper left and a small one lower right; or a white eye ringed in ink with a pupil looking a little inward), **shut** (a gentle curve, for blinks and fainting), **squeezed** (a chevron pointing to the nose, when hit) and **fierce** (a lid pressed toward the inner corner, painted in the colour of the skin round the eye, while attacking) and **smiling** (the lid shut in an arch, its middle raised, the shut curve turned over: a cheer, plan 25 · Y5; a species whose eyes are always closed smiles too). A species whose eyes are always closed (Abra, Snorlax) shows them shut in every state but the squeeze; one that always glares (the Gastly line, Honchkrow) shows them fierce whenever they are open. A white eye's white can be another colour (Hoothoot's red, Spiritomb's lime). Markings are dots, rings, four- and five-pointed stars, bars, wavy lines, zigzags, triangles, diamonds and smiles (a shallow arc, its ends turned up: Ditto's, Lapras's, Dragonite's and Mew's mouths).
- **Hand-built models** (plan 03 · D6–D9, and decision 3 since): Platinum's Sinnoh Pokédex is sculpted species by species, and the popular species from outside it follow batch by batch from Kanto, Johto, Hoenn, Unova, Kalos, Alola, the rest of Sinnoh, Galar, Hisui and Paldea, our own sculpts after each design's shapes, colours and proportions, with the same kit and rules. Membranes and fins are thin: a bat's wing is skin over its arm and finger bones, filled triangle by triangle from the wrist and scalloped between the fingers; an insect's or a fish's fin is a flat oval rimmed in a darker or paler colour. Rock is lumps over a body (Geodude's line) or a chain of separate boulders with a crease between each (Onix); honeycomb is hexagonal cells. A wing whose two faces are coloured differently is two membranes laid back to back, its finger bones in the back one so the front stays clean (Charizard's, teal in front and orange behind). A lightning bolt is a flat zigzag (Pikachu's line), a curl a tube wound flat into a spiral (the Clefairy line's foreheads), whiskers thin pale tubes. A leaf with a jagged edge is a blade with leaflets swept past its edges (Ivysaur's and Venusaur's palm leaves); a snake rests on its coils laid on the ground (the Ekans line); a flame is lit from within, pale at its heart. A spiral on a belly is painted in short strokes along its curve (the Poliwag line); a crab's claw is a swollen hand and two fingers bowed apart round an open gap, closing at their tips (Krabby's line); a bell's or a pitcher's mouth is a hollow carved in and painted dark, a fleshy lip round it (Bellsprout's line); spots on a tilted cap are painted where its surface lies (Paras's line); shaggy fur is tufts of points over a body, bent down as far as the fur hangs (Venonat, Galarian Meowth, the Mankey line, the Growlithe line's manes). Fins, leaves and crests that come to a point are blades, flat to the side they face (Remoraid's fins, Leafeon's leaves, Gallade's crest); a leaf or a frond that is widest in the middle is an oval flat to the side it faces (Tropius's wings). Vines are tubes looped over a ball, the face left bare (Tangela's line); fur that hangs is points pointing down round a body (Piloswine's line); a tiger's stripes are short bands painted across the surface, never rings round it (Electabuzz's line); a drill is a cone with grooves round it (Rhydon's line); a body of flat faces is blocks joined without blending (Porygon). An ammonite's spiral is painted along its shell's sides in short strokes, with lines across its back where its chambers meet (the Omanyte line); a seahorse's tail curls forward into a spiral under it (the Horsea line); a star stands on edge, its five points flattened front to back and the jewel at its core set in a ring of gold (Staryu's line; Starmie's second star is turned half a point behind the first). A bird's spread wing is an arm with long feathers fanned from it, rounded or pointed like spikes (the legendary birds), and a streamer is a flat ribbon of pieces that reach well past each other, so its edges hardly dip where they meet (Articuno's tail). Long narrow leaves arch from a crown (Exeggutor's). White down a serpent's rising neck is painted a stroke on each of the neck's bones, since paint colours only its own bone (Dratini's line). A flower is petals round a disc, flat to the way it faces: up from a head (Skiploom), forward as a face (Sunflora), two in the hair (Bellossom). Coral is branches with round tips and a twig forking off each (Corsola). A puffer's spikes stand all over it but for its face and the root of its tail (Qwilfish). The legendary beasts stand on long legs with broad paws, a storm cloud a row of lumps over the back (Raikou), a flowing mane a flat ribbon (Suicune), smoke puffs of three rounds (Entei). A wing that ends in fingers is feathers spread from its wrist (Lugia); antlers are tubes with tines forking off them (Stantler). A lily pad is a flat disc with a rim, its wedges marks on its top (the Lotad line); an acorn's cap is ringed in painted steps (Seedot); a halo floats free over its wearer (Shedinja, which is in two pieces by design); an eye on a wing is a mark ringed in paint (Masquerain). A crescent is a disc with a smaller disc carved from its side (Lunatone); a sun's rays are blades fanned round a ball in its own plane (Solrock); a spring is a tube wound in a helix (Spoink); a cut gem is a flattened oval with a paler ring on its face (Sableye's eyes and chest). A golem of stone or ice is blocks joined with little rounding, and its face seven dots in its own pattern (the Regis); a body of steel plates is discs and limbs with grey joints (the Beldum line); strands twisted together are two tubes wound round one path (Deoxys's arms); a crescent of wings is a flat disc with its middle carved away before the body goes in (Mega Salamence). A drill is a cone banded round with paint (Mega Excadrill); stone is blocks with rounded edges, crystals jutting from it (the Roggenrola line); a crest of an element is its own shape on one build (the elemental monkeys: a tuft of leaves, a curl or row of flame, rounds of water). A thing carried is held on the bone of the hand that holds it and touches both hands (Timburr's log, Gurdurr's beam, Conkeldurr's pillars), or on the tail that holds it (Yamask's mask); a hood is a shell cut open at the face, the face added after the cut and the cut wide enough to show the whole face (Sewaddle's leaf, Mega Scrafty's, Chespin's); a centipede curled into a wheel looks out of a hollow carved in its middle (Whirlipede); rock in layers is a block painted in thin flat bands (Crustle); brows of flame are a row of tongues, not a horn (Darmanitan). A cell in gel is a pale body set in a ball of green gel, its face standing out of the gel's front (the Solosis line); a gear is a steel ring with square teeth round a sunken face and a raised core, and gears that mesh stand in one plane (the Klink line); a jellyfish's tentacles are broad ribbons hanging from its collar (Frillish's line); a gown in tiers is flared cones cut flat at the ground (Gothitelle); a season is one build in the season's colours with what it wears on its antlers (Deerling and Sawsbuck). A seal of clay is a square panel with a spiral pressed into it (the Golett line); a head hidden in fur is a hood of shaggy tufts over an open jaw, and a head at the end of an arm the same hood made smaller (the Deino line); a moth's six wings are flat petals round it in one plane, kept narrow enough to stand apart (Volcarona); the genies of the forces of nature ride a heap of white puffs on the root bone, their arms crossed in front of them (Tornadus, Thundurus, Landorus); a turbine is a drum ringed with bands round a tail's middle (Reshiram, the White Kyurem) and a generator a drum at a tail's end, its rim lit (Zekrom, the Black Kyurem); a fusion is one build with the other dragon's wings and tail (Kyurem's); a drive is the colour of a slot on one build (Genesect). A shuriken of water is a flat disc with blades fanned in its own plane (Ash-Greninja's, the one Mega Greninja hangs from); a butterfly's patterns are one build in each pattern's colours, its wings banded round their roots, spotted and rimmed (Vivillon); a flower a fairy holds or sits on is petals round a disc on the bone that bears it, and a wreath is rounds of flowers about the head with strands of small ones hanging on a thread (the Flabébé line); a trim is one build with the trim's shapes on the head and tail and its colour at the legs (Furfrou); a haunted sword's eye is on the front of its crossguard and its cloth arcs over the pommel and falls beside the blade to a frayed end (the Honedge line), and a shield held in front touches the cloths that hold it (Aegislash). A hand with a face of its own carries its own eyes (Binacle's two hands, four eyes in all); a slime is a puddle of a darker shade with the body rising out of it (the Goomy line), and a shell of metal over it a smooth gray dome with a spiral drawn on each side (the Hisuian Sliggoo and Goodra); a jack-o'-lantern's face is marks that glow, never carved (Gourgeist); the sizes of a Pokémon that comes in sizes are one build at each size's scale and fill (the Pumpkaboo line); a shrimp's great claw stands to one side of its face, never over its eyes (Clauncher's line); a sail down a neck is a curtain of fins, widest behind the head (Aurorus); antlers of light are a beam with tines, each tine tipped in its own colour (Xerneas); a hand that floats free is a piece of its own on a bone of its own, as Haunter's (Hoopa Unbound's four). A Pokémon of several styles is one build in each style's colours and dress (Oricorio's four); a school is one great fish whose skin is many small fish, each laid on the surface and tied to it by a short stalk so the school stays one piece (Wishiwashi's School Form); a hood of leaves is green paint over the back of the head with two leaves falling from its crown to its cheeks (Dartrix); a cage of legs is tubes arched from the crown down to the ground, each on a bone of its own, the front ones parted to show the face inside (Toxapex). A Pokémon that carries a memory or shows a core is one build in its colour (Silvally's fin and eyes, Minior's cores); a disguise whose neck is broken is the same head turned about the neck, its scrawled face and ears turning with it (the busted Mimikyu); armour of scales is discs laid on the surface along its normal, each with a heart painted at its middle (the Jangmo-o line). A Pokémon fused with another is the other's build, its colours changed where the design changes them and the fusing one's armour laid over it (Necrozma's Dusk Mane and Dawn Wings); a hex nut is six slabs, each reaching exactly to the corners, where the next takes over, or the corners stand out like a cog's teeth (Meltan and Melmetal). A Pokémon that holds a plate is one build in its type's colours, its grey and the gems on its wheel taking the type's (Arceus), and a wheel round a body is a ring tipped toward the head with four arms crossing it into the body, so it stays one piece; smoke and cloud are a heap of round puffs, never a column (Mega Heatran); a drum's face is a disc laid on a flattened ellipsoid, never a capsule's rounded end (the Gigantamax Rillaboom); a rider on what it has become is a small copy of its build standing in it (the Gigantamax Cinderace on its fireball, the Gigantamax Inteleon on its tower); a flower worn as a bonnet tips back so the face shows under its brim (Gossifleur), and a face set in front of a great ball is lighter than the leaves that frame it, so it doesn't sink into them (Eldegoss). A cup is a round bowl cut off flat at its rim and hollowed, its tea filling the hollow down to its bottom, or the hollow under the tea is a closed pocket that the soundness check counts as a loose piece (Sinistea); a pie's lattice is strips laid along the crust's own surface both ways (Appletun); a centipede is segments each ringed with a darker band, a pair of little legs on each that touches the ground (the Sizzlipede line); a twister is a tube winding up and out from a point (the Gigantamax Sandaconda); a catch is held between the open jaws of the beak, on the head's bone (Cramorant's). A Pokémon of many creams and sweets is one build in each cream's colours with the sweet it wears tucked into each lock, a swirled cream striped round its locks, its peak and its skirt (Alcremie's 63); a squad is its troopers in a line turned a little aside, so that each one's face clears the one in front (Falinks); a moth's wings face forward, as a butterfly's do, or the camera sees them edge on (Frosmoth); a face on a flat block keeps its decals clear of the pillars and crown that join the block, by more than the reach of their blend (Stonjourner). A flier's hat like a stealth plane is blades flat to the sky (Drakloak, Dragapult), and a little one riding on it a small copy of its build, its eyes kept to the middle of its small face so their squares lie on the face all round (the Dreepys); a rider on a mount is a small copy of its build seated on the mount's back on the body's bone, reins a thin tube from its hands to the mount's muzzle (Calyrex's Ice and Shadow Riders); a war horse of ice stands in clusters of crystal points round each hoof (Glastrier), and a mane or tail of smoke is wavy strands streaming down (Spectrier); a spring is rings stacked down a cone, each touching it, or they fall apart (Regieleki); a building of a Pokémon is blocks with little rounding, a wall of glass a flat point down its front (Duraludon and its Gigantamax form); a braid is a chain of small rounds along the body (Zacian), a crowned hero's armour gold plates laid over the build (the Crowned Sword and Shield). A heart drawn in the air is a thin loop on the heart's own curve standing on its point on the head, and a heart on the skin two lobes of paint leaning together to a point (Enamorus); a cloak of peat is a grey dome over the back with brown flaps hanging round its edge (Ursaluna); an axe held on a forearm is a narrow blade turned aside, or from the front it reads as a wing (Kleavor); a boa of water is a string of beads close enough to touch, or it falls apart (Quaquaval); a ball of thread is a white ball with lines of thread painted round it every way (Tarountula), and thread held between legs thin tubes from knee to knee and down (Spidops). A family is its members as one piece, holding paws, the ears of two side by side staggered front to back so they never touch, and a little one's eyes a little bigger for its size, so they stay whole on its small face (Tandemaus, Maushold); an ear rolled up like a bun is a thick ring on its edge filled with a softer roll (Fidough, Dachsbun); salt is blocks with little rounding, banded brown and white like strata, its eyes orange slits (the Nacli line); a tumbleweed is a cage of branches arching up from a knot at its foot, its eyes held up inside it on a stem so it stays one piece, and a wreath of branches rings laid a little askew (Bramblin, Brambleghast); a blade of ghostly fire carries a thin ridge down its middle, which keeps its point whole (Ceruledge). Two heads on one body are each on a bone of their own, a neck to each (Scovillain); what a Pokémon holds up or pushes along is joined to it by its limbs, so the two stay one piece (Rellor's ball, Rabsca's); a great hammer is a thick cylinder capped flat at both ends, on the bone of the hand that holds it (Tinkaton); a garden eel is a tube rising from the ground and bowing over at its top (the Wiglett line); a bud of crystal that bursts open stands its petals round it on stalks of crystal, so it stays one piece (Mega Glimmora); ports along a worm of many segments are painted, since its segments overlap and a decal there finds no one bone under it (Orthworm). An eye's half-height is at least a cell and a half: a model as tall as Miraidon (1.44) has cells of 0.012 and eyes of 0.017, and smaller ones find too few triangles to cover their square. A creature sitting in a bowl reaches down into the hollow the bowl's cut leaves, or it floats there as a piece of its own (Sinistcha); a chain is links each touching the last, and a collar of links ends at the link the chain leaves it from (Okidogi). The mesh is 128 cells across a model's largest dimension, so a very tall design is kept no taller than its look needs and its thin parts at least a cell thick (Alolan Exeggutor). Porygon-Z's parts float apart, as the design has them, and so does Shedinja's halo. Fliers and floaters hang 12 % of their height above their platform, as the generated ones do. Eyes follow the design: two as a rule; none for Zubat, for Ralts (under its hair), for Nosepass (shut in a black band), for Yanmega (its eyes are its head), for Piloswine (under its fur), for Deino and Zweilous (under their hoods of fur) for Staryu's line (its core is its face), for Chinchou (its crossed eyes are marks) for the Regis, Regigigas, Regieleki and Regidrago (dots in their place), for Type: Null (behind its mask), for the riders of the Gigantamax Cinderace and Inteleon (too small for eyes to show), for the Gigantamax Flapple and Sandaconda (an apple and a twister, no face showing) and the Gigantamax Alcremie (a cake), for Nihilego, Xurkitree, Celesteela, Kartana and Blacephalon (no face at all), for Eternatus and its Eternamax (a skull with no eyes showing) for Roggenrola and Boldore (the hexagon on their front is an ear), for the Nacli line (orange slits, marks that never blink), for Bramblin and Brambleghast (spindles held in their branches), for Orthworm (only its mouth), for Greavard (under its fur), for Houndstone (hollows in its skull) and for Poltchageist and Sinistcha (swirls painted on caddy, bowl and matcha); one for Lunatone, Beldum, Honedge and Aegislash (on the crossguard; Doublade has one on each sword), Dhelmise (a porthole on its wheel), Meltan and Melmetal (in the hole of their nut), Trevenant (in a dark hollow of its trunk), Sigilyph (the two eyespots on its body are marks, which never blink), Litwick (wax over the other), Darkrai and its Mega (the other under the plume), Rolycoly (its one great eye), Runerigus (in the top of its slab), Klang's and Klinklang's small gear (the great gear's face is marks), Unown, Kirlia (the other under its hair), Magnemite, Charcadet (the other under its shell of charcoal) and the Duskull line; three for Magneton and Magnezone and for Sandy Shocks (one on each of its units); four for Vanilluxe's two faces, for Drakloak (its own and the Dreepy's on its hat) for Calyrex's Ice and Shadow Riders (the horse's and Calyrex's) for Tandemaus (two mice) and for Scovillain and its Mega (a pair on each head); six for Dragapult (its own and its two Dreepys'), for Wugtrio (a pair on each of its three), for Iron Jugulis (its head's and the heads' on its arms) and for Maushold's family of three, eight for its family of four; Joltik's and Galvantula's small eyes, which never blink, are marks; seven round Claydol's head; a pair on each of Combee's three faces, on each of Dugtrio's and Dodrio's three heads and on Doduo's two, on each of Exeggcute's six eggs and Exeggutor's three heads (four for the Alolan, one on its tail), on each of Weezing's two heads and Binacle's two hands, and on Kangaskhan and its baby.
- **Generated models** (plan 03 · D5): every species without a hand-built model is sculpted from its data with the same kit, the same rules (smooth bodies, paint for patches, decals for eyes and marks, the materials below) and the same body plans. Chibi proportions shrink with evolution: basic species and babies have the biggest heads and shortest legs, final stages and legendaries the longest. Colours: the Pokédex colour as the body, nudged in hue and value by the evolutionary line so families share a shade; an accent from the types (a flame's orange, a leaf's green) where it stands apart from the body; a pale belly and muzzle; dark paws, stripes and masks. Fliers and floaters hang 12 % of their height above their platform.
- **Forms** (plan 03 · D11): a form (a Mega, a regional form, a Gigantamax form, Deoxys's formes) is a model of its own, generated as its species would be from the form's types, stats and size and in the form's own Pokédex colour (black for Mega Charizard X, white for Alolan Vulpix), as one of its species' family, by Platinum's values whatever rules the game is played by. Every form of a hand-built species is hand-built like the species. Platinum's own: Rotom's appliances are the appliance in Rotom's orange with Rotom's eyes, its plasma limbs in the appliance's own colour; Giratina's Origin Forme floats as a banded serpent with six shadowy tendrils; the cloaks and the East Sea keep their species' build in other materials and colours; the Unown's letters are the A's eye in its ring with strokes of the same black in the front plane, the signs (! and ?) with the eye half shut. The later games': a regional form keeps its species' build in its region's colours and materials; a Mega is its species grown into its Mega Evolution's shape (Mega Alakazam's five spoons float round its head, as the design has them); a Gigantamax form carries Gigantamax's red clouds over it, puffs strung on a wisp so they hang together; Pikachu wears its caps and costumes over its own sculpt, a female's heart at the tip of its tail with the costumes. A form that looks just like its species (Mothim's cloaks, the partner Pikachu and Eevee) shows its sculpt, and a model file named after a form (`Charizard-Mega-X.glb`) replaces any of them.
- **Model files** (`overrides/models`, [`docs/model-files.md`](../model-files.md)): a glTF model replaces a species' sculpt as it is, lit by the character shader with material 0 (or glow and metal where its materials say), outlined by a shell half a sprite pixel out along smoothed normals, scaled to the same size rule.
- **Alphas** (plan 10 · F10, `Overworld/Alphas.cs`; rulings, "Alphas"): an alpha is its species' own model at one and a half times the size (`Pokemon.Scale`, `Alphas.Scale`), drawn so in battle by `BattleRenderer` (the model's scale times the giant's times the Pokémon's own, which is 1 for everyone else and leaves the product to the bit), its lunge, shake and footing going with it; the camera frames it by its larger size as it does a giant. Its eyes are held fierce while it stands in battle (number 5 of the eyes' order below): a held look the renderer is told of (`PokemonRenderer.Draw`'s `fierce`, which `BattleRenderer` sets for an alpha), never a field of the pose, so no sculpt and no other Pokémon's eyes change. Its field sprite is the species' own drawn at the same 1.5 (`NPC.Scale`, which `Follower.Dress` takes from the Pokémon, so a wild alpha in sight and an alpha that follows the player stand at it), not a sprite of its own (so the sprite's signature is the species', and no cache moves): the card is one and a half times as wide and as tall, its foot where a plain one's is, its texels a texel and a half; at 1 the card is the one it always was, to the bit. Its cry is the species' lowered five semitones, deeper and a third longer (`Cries.Alpha`). Its mark is Alpha, the only mark it carries. Nothing in the picture of a Pokémon that is no alpha changes.
- **Props worn** (plan 42 · PS1; `Graphics/ShowPropModels.cs`, `Graphics/PropAnchors.cs`; our own designs, none a game's): the stage show's pieces (a top hat, a paper crown, round glasses, a bow tie, a scarf, a short cape, a folding fan, a baton, a ribbon for the tail and the rest of `Data/show-props.json`) are sculpted with the same kit as the bodies, in the piece's two colours (body and trim), each part at least two mesh cells thick, outlined by a shell of their own as a body is. They are worn on the Pokémon's own model at an anchor found on its mesh, never guessed: the top of the head's surface (the highest up-facing point near the head's middle, so a crest or horn doesn't lift the hat) for a hat, the face's front between the eyes for glasses and masks, the front of the seam where head meets body for a neck piece (a ring piece fitted to the seam's own width and depth), the back's highest point for a cape or wings, the end of an arm for what is held, the tip of the last tail bone for a tail piece. Each anchor rides its bone through every pose. Pieces are scaled by the head: a unit of a piece's sculpt is the head's half width, so a hat sits on a Pikachu as on a Garchomp. A body that lacks a slot (a hand on a fish or a quadruped, a tail on a body with no tail bone, a head on a body whose face is none of its own and has no eyes) is not offered it; a model file offers none. The kit's materials: cloth (`Cloth`) for hats, bows, scarves, capes and ribbons, paper as matt cloth, `Metal` for crowns, tiaras, bells, the monocle's rim and the wand's star, `Plastic` for glasses' frames, lenses and beads, `Leather` for a baton's grip. The moods' colours: bright pieces in sunny yellows and oranges, dashing ones in reds and navy with black, gentle ones in pinks, mints and pale blues, eerie ones in violets and teals. **Props are worn only on the stage and in the dressing room**: never in battle, the field, a menu's sprite or the Pokédex, and a bake that is handed no pieces is the picture it always was, to the bit (its signature unmoved).
- **Shiny Pokémon** (plan 09 · L1; `Graphics/ShinyPalettes.cs`). A shiny Pokémon is its own model in other colours, never another sculpt: the same mesh, its vertex colours mapped as it is put on the GPU, and its decal atlas painted again in the mapped colours. Its look is named by its model's name with `+s` after it (`Gyarados+s`, `Charizard-Mega-X+s`: `Pokemon.LookName`, `PokemonLook`), and the menu sprites, the field sprites and every cache of what is made from a model key by that name, so nothing drawn of a Pokémon that isn't shiny changes. The mesh cache is shared: the mesh is the same.
  - **The rule**, for every species and form the table doesn't name: every colour with a saturation of 0.15 or more and a value of 0.16 or more is turned round the hue circle by an angle of the species' own, 60° + (47 × its National number mod 240)°, so never less than 60° nor more than 300° (from the number, never from a string's hash); its saturation and value stay. Greys, whites and darks stay as they are, and so do the eyes (the iris, the pupil and the sclera): a shiny is the same creature in another coat. Where the body's **main colour** (the colour of the most vertices of its mesh) is itself a grey, a white or a black, it is tinted instead: its saturation raised to 0.3 in the hue of that angle (a black lifted to a value of 0.22, or the tint wouldn't show), with every colour within 24 of it in each channel (an eyelid painted in it). A form takes its species' number.
  - **The table**, for the designs whose shiny is best known (decision 2: some forty by hand, the rest by rule, growing as bad ones are seen), gives a species rules of its own, each a band of hues turned to a hue of ours with its saturation and value scaled, and for a grey body a hue and a saturation it takes: a red Gyarados (its blue to 355°), a gold Magikarp, a black Charizard with crimson in its wings, the three Sinnoh starters' lines lighter and yellower (Turtwig's green toward 60°, Chimchar's orange toward 45°, Piplup's blue toward 260°), a yellow Shinx line and a yellow Riolu and Lucario, a silver Eevee and Metagross, a blue-ringed Umbreon, a black Rayquaza, a pale blue-grey Giratina. No two palettes of one line are the same: each step of a line turns a few degrees further. A colour the table's rules leave alone stays, but a main colour they leave alone takes the rule, so the main colour always changes.
  - **The marks** follow the body: a decal's colour and the eyelid painted in the colour under it are mapped like the mesh; its iris, pupil, sclera and glints are not.
  - **A model file** (`overrides/models`, `assets/models`) is shiny by the same rule, put through its textures: its base colour image is copied with every colour passed through the species' palette as it goes to the GPU (the file and its decoded images are never changed), and so are its vertex colours and its outline shell. Its **main colour** is the colour of the most sampled texels of its images (at most 16,384 an image, opaque ones only), because a file's vertices are mostly white; a palette of the table or the rule then reads as for a sculpt. The shiny's sprite signature is marked `file`, so a sprite baked while a file's shiny still looked plain is never found again.
  - **The sparkle** as a shiny comes out (`CueKind.Shiny`, our own after the original's beat): once the Pokémon has grown out of its ball (1.0 s after the throw), or 0.5 s after a wild one appears, eight four-pointed stars burst out of its middle in a ring and fly out to 0.75 of its height over 0.5 s, blinking off for one step in every three steps of 0.08 s, while a pale gold glow swells and fades behind them, and a second ring of five smaller stars follows 0.25 s later; 1.1 s from first to last, pale gold (255, 236, 150) and white, at most 16 quads, nothing over the screen and no shake. The sound is `shiny`, a run of bright bell notes, as the first stars burst. It plays wherever a send-out or a wild Pokémon's appearing plays, but not with the Battle Scene off, where the original's other animations that are no moves are left out too, and not about a Pokémon behind a doll.
- **Female looks** (plan 09 · L6 to L8; `PokemonLook.Females`, `PokemonModels.CreateFemale`). Where a species' females look different (PokeAPI's flag, `genderDifferences`: a flag, never a picture), the female is drawn as her own look, named by her model's name with `+f` after it, before any `+s` (`Pikachu+f`, `Pikachu+f+s`: `Pokemon.LookName`). She is the species' own sculptor asked for the female, which changes only what differs and leaves the male's sculpt as it was to the bit, so no male's mesh or sprite is made again; her signature is marked as hers, so her sprites bake under keys of their own, and her mesh shares the male's cache entry wherever only her decals differ. A difference of shape is sculpted (a smaller horn, a heart cut in a tail, a bigger abdomen, a shorter moustache); a difference of colour or of a mark is paint or a decal on the same shapes (a paler neck, white whiskers, bigger spots). Each is the original's difference in our own shapes and colours, never bigger than it: she must read as the same species at a glance, and as a female only to someone who looks. A model file (`overrides/models`) shows its own shapes for her too, until a file of her own (`Pikachu+f.glb`) is made. A shiny female is her own sculpt in her species' shiny palette. Kanto's 23 (L6):

  | Species | The female |
  |---|---|
  | Venusaur | a pale green seed in the middle of her flower, on its crown |
  | Butterfree | a black spot on each lower wing |
  | Rattata, Raticate | whiskers about two thirds as long |
  | Pikachu | the tail's square end notched in its middle and rounded at its corners, a heart in outline, still yellow (the costumes keep their own heart, painted black) |
  | Raichu | the bolt on the tail stops short and ends round, without its point |
  | Zubat, Golbat | fangs two thirds as long |
  | Gloom, Vileplume | the spots on the petals half as big again |
  | Kadabra | whiskers that stop at the cheek; Alakazam: a moustache that ends at the chin |
  | Doduo, Dodrio | necks beige (214, 186, 146) where the male's are near black |
  | Hypno | a bigger, fuller collar |
  | Rhyhorn, Rhydon, Goldeen, Seaking | a smaller horn (about two thirds as long, a little thinner) |
  | Scyther | a bigger abdomen, swelling out behind |
  | Magikarp | white whiskers where the male's are yellow |
  | Gyarados | white whiskers where the male's are pale blue (the male's were white before L6, which is the female's look) |
  | Eevee | the cream at the tail's tip a heart: two lobes over a point, a cleft of brown at the top |

  Johto's 22 and Hoenn's 18 (L7):

  | Ledyba, Ledian | antennae shorter, their tips nearer the head |
  | Xatu | one band fewer low on her cloak (no black band over the red) |
  | Sudowoodo | a smaller branch on her head |
  | Politoed | a smaller curl on her head, on a shorter stalk |
  | Aipom | a shorter tuft of hair |
  | Wooper | smaller gills |
  | Quagsire | a smaller, thinner fin along the tail |
  | Murkrow | a smaller hat, its crown shorter |
  | Wobbuffet | red lips round the mouth, like lipstick |
  | Girafarig | the brown of the hindquarters reaching further forward |
  | Gligar | a smaller stinger |
  | Steelix | teeth of another shape: two square teeth rising from the front of her jaw |
  | Scizor | a fuller abdomen, swelling out behind |
  | Heracross | the horn ending in a heart (two lobes, a cleft at the top, a point into the stem) where the male's is forked |
  | Sneasel | a shorter feather off her ear |

  | Piloswine, Donphan | shorter tusks |
  | Octillery | a smaller head |
  | Houndoom | shorter horns, wound less far |
  | Torchic | no black spot low on her back, which the male has (the male's spot came with L7) |
  | Combusken | a smaller crest |
  | Blaziken | shorter feathers on her head |
  | Beautifly | smaller red spots on her upper wings |

  | Medicham | a smaller bulb atop her headdress |

  | Gulpin | a shorter feather on her head |
  | Swalot | shorter whiskers |
  | Numel | a bigger hump |
  | Camerupt | bigger volcanoes on her back |
  | Cacturne | pale diamonds in a line down her front, where the male's are dark and scattered |

  Sinnoh's 31 and Unfezant (L8):

  | Species | The female |

  | Starly, Staravia, Staraptor | a smaller white forehead: the white of the mask lower over the brow |
  | Bidoof | a smaller, plainer tail, where the male's has more to it |

  | Kricketot | a bigger collar, wider and taller behind the head |

  | Hippowdon | the male's colours turned about: a grey hide plated in sandy yellow |
  | Croagunk | smaller poison sacs on the cheeks; Toxicroak: a smaller sac at the throat |
  | Finneon | a bigger tail, its two lobes longer; Lumineon: bigger lower fins |
  | Snover | more of the trunk under snow, the coat's hem lower; Abomasnow: longer fur down the front and in the beard |
  | Weavile | a smaller crown of feathers |
  | Rhyperior | smaller horns, the drill and the horn above it |
  | Tangrowth | bigger hands, the red fingers longer and thicker |
  | Mamoswine | smaller tusks |
  | Unfezant | a brown hen with a paler breast: no pink wattles round the eyes, and a short brown tuft where the male's long pink plume curls |

  The females of Frillish, Jellicent, Pyroar, Meowstic, Indeedee, Basculegion and Oinkologne are forms of their own (`-Female`), in which a female of those species is from the start, so they need no `+f`. **A flagged species nobody has sculpted** (none today; the net for a species a later batch forgets) has a female by rule: her male's genome with the head, and whatever grows on it, made smaller by `PokemonGenomes.FemaleHeadScale` (0.92), the commonest of the originals' differences (`PokemonGenomes.FemaleOf`). `PokemonLook.HasFemale` is the list and every flagged species with no female form. The Pokédex's FORMS page (plan 08 · P19) shows a female seen in her own look from the front and from behind (`SeenLook.Look`); the INFO page has no toggle.

- **Clips** (`PokemonAnimation`), layered over the idle loop: *idle* (breathing, head sway, tail swish, ear flicks every few seconds, wings flapping when airborne, flames flickering); *physical* (drawn back, then a strike forward with the jaw open, arms swung through and a lunge across the field); *special* (rears up gathering power with arms and wings spread, then thrusts toward the target with a cry, without moving from its place); *status* (a hop and a nod); *hit* (recoil, squash, limbs flung out); *faint* (the legs give way, then a biped topples onto its front, a quadruped sinks onto its belly with its legs splayed, a flier drops onto its side; then it sinks into the platform); *entry* (lands with a squash out of the ball, then stands tall with a cry); *walk* (plan 10 · F2: `PokePose.Walk`, the stride's phase 0..1, two steps to a stride, blended in by `PokePose.Moving`, over the idle at half its strength; one stride covers two tiles in the field): a biped's legs swing in turn, 0.45 rad either way, its arms against them and its body rolling a little over the foot it stands on; a quadruped trots, each leg against the one before or behind it on its side and with the one across from it on the other; a bird hops (however it hovers at rest), twice a stride, a twentieth of its height, its legs tucked and its wings flicked in the air; a serpent and a fish undulate, a wave of yaw from the tail to the head along the segments' phases; any other body that hovers (and every floater) keeps its height and leans a tenth into its way, wings beating faster, legs trailing. The root never rises off the ground but in a bird's hop. An imported model plays its own *walk* or *run* clip where it has one. A physical strike and a special move's release peak when the damage lands, 0.35 s into a 0.65 s move.
  - **Bodies that answer** (plan 25 · Y5; Platinum draws none of these, so they are our own): *dodge*, as a move that misses passes it, 0.8 s with its peak at the impact (0.35 s): a biped or a quadruped hops aside, a bird banks and slips aside, a fish or a floater dips and slips aside, a serpent coils, drawing its head in and leaning aside; each comes back to where it stood. The way aside is away from the miss's path (a miss flies wide on one side of its target; the dodge takes the other), a third of the body's height for one that hops, a quarter for one that banks or dips and a fifth for a serpent, which mostly coils. *Tired*, for as long as its HP bar is in the red (a fifth or less, as the bar shows it, never the rules' HP): the head lowers, the arms, wings, ears and tail droop, a flier hangs lower, and a slow deep breath (one every 2.6 s) takes over from most of the idle's; it eases in and out over 0.6 s as the bar crosses the line. *Cheer*, 1.2 s: two hops with the head thrown back and the arms, wings and tail up, for the player's Pokémon on the field as the battle is won or a Pokémon caught, and for the foe's as the player loses. *Charge*, through a charge turn (Solar Beam's light, Skull Bash's tuck, Sky Attack's glow), from the line that tells of it until the move is let go next turn: the body gathers itself low, the head down and drawn in, the arms and wings folded, a little tremble in it; it eases in over 0.4 s and goes as the move's own lunge begins, and a type's aura gathers round it (the move's charge half; each charge move's own comes with its batch of Y6 to Y10). Every one starts and ends at the idle pose with the feet where they were.
- **The pose's fields and the eyes' order** (plan 09 · L2 keeps this list; plan 25 · Y5 and plan 10 · F2, F6 and F10 add to it in the places given here, whichever lands first).
  - `PokePose`'s fields stand in groups, in this order, and a new field goes at the end of its own group, never between two that are there: the clock (`Time`); the layered clips (`Blink`, `Attack`, `Kind`, `Hurt`, `Faint`, `Entry`); what lasts on the body (`Asleep`, `Frozen`: L2); a battle's answers (`Dodge`, `Tired`, `Cheer`, `Charge`, `Stat`: Y5; the dodge and the cheer are their clips' progress, tiredness and the charge how much of them shows, and `Stat` a stat's change within its 0.6 s, above nought for a rise and below for a fall); the walk (`Walk`, `Moving`: F2); the camp's faces (`Happy`, `Sleepy`, `LookAt`: F6, built by plan 35 · BN2, and `Eating` at its end: `Happy` how far it leans into a stroke, `Sleepy` how far it droops, `LookAt` the way its head turns toward the hand, x to its right and y up, each −1 to 1, and `Eating` how much it chews). With all four at nought the pose is what it was, to the bit. A held look (F10) is no field: the renderer is told of it beside the pose (`EyesOf(pose, fierce)`).
  - The eyes (`PokemonAnimation.EyesOf`) show the first of these that holds:
    1. a faint, past a fifth of its clip: shut;
    2. asleep: shut, whatever else happens to it (a sleeper that is hit, or that strikes in its sleep with Snore or Sleep Talk, keeps them shut);
    3. a hit: squeezed;
    4. an attack that is no status move, or a charge (Y5): fierce;
    5. a held fierce look (F10: an alpha standing in battle, `EyesOf`'s `fierce`): fierce;
    6. a stat falling, squeezed for 0.6 s, or rising, fierce for 0.6 s (Y5);
    7. frozen: open and held, and nothing below this line reaches a frozen face;
    8. a cheer (Y5): smiling, shut in a smile's curve; then the camp's faces (F6, BN2): happy past 0.3, smiling; eating past 0.3, shut on each bite (the first half of every chew of 0.4 s); sleepy past 0.5, shut for 1.2 s in every 3 s, in place of the blink below;
    9. tiredness (Y5): longer blinks, shut for 0.45 s in every 2.6 s, in place of the blink below;
    10. a blink: shut;
    11. open.
  - A species' own eyes go over this as before (one whose eyes are always closed, one that always glares).
  - A frozen body is held the same way: its idle loop is played at one moment whatever the clock says (the loop's start), so it neither breathes, sways nor flaps, and a flier hangs still. A clip that answers something (a hit's recoil, the faint) still plays over the held idle.

### Materials

Character and Pokémon surfaces carry a material (G6, G7), which the character shader lights differently. Highlights are one crisp band where the half vector meets the normal (`smoothstep(0.42, 0.5, …)`), never a smooth gradient.

| Material | Terminator (`smoothstep(0, w, N·L)`) | Highlight band (strength, sharpness) | Own light | Shade |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Default | 0.12 | none | none | as lit (models without materials) |
| Skin | 0.22 | none | none | warm: sky light × `1.10, 0.96, 0.94` where unlit |
| Cloth | 0.16 | none | none | rim × 0.9 |
| Hair | 0.10 | 0.14, 10 (a soft sheen) | none | |
| Leather (shoes, bags) | 0.08 | 0.40, 36 | none | |
| Plastic (soles, brims, buttons) | 0.10 | 0.35, 48 | none | |
| Metal (buckles, clasps) | 0.06 | 0.60, 64 | none | slightly cool |
| Glow (the Rift, flames) | 0.12 | none | 0.85 | rim × 0.5 |
| Fur and feathers | 0.20 | none | none | a touch warm, rim × 1.15 |
| Scales and smooth hide | 0.12 | 0.12, 26 | none | |
| Shell (shells, claws, beaks, teeth, horns) | 0.08 | 0.36, 30 | none | rim × 0.9 |
| Leaf | 0.22 | 0.06, 8 | none | green bounce `0.94, 1.06, 0.94`, rim × 1.25 |

### Stage

- Textures are smooth, mipmapped and filtered (`SoftTextures`): meadow `114,190,100` with light `138,202,104` and deep `90,168,94` patches a few metres across; platform tops lighter in the middle (`150,220,124` → `100,182,96`) with one soft worn ring.
- Foliage is modelled (`SoftFoliage`): tiered pines with drooping rims and dark undersides; round trees made of overlapping blobs whose normals bend toward the canopy centre so the crown shades as one soft mass; seven-blade tufts, dark at the root, light at the tip. Colour comes from vertex gradients.
- Scenery takes a soft cel band (`BattleRamp` 0.85).
- **Places on a platform** (plan 14 · B4, `Graphics/BattlePlaces.cs`): one Pokémon stands at its platform's middle, whole; two stand half the platform's radius left and right of it, at 0.78 (the player's first on the left, the foe's first on the right, as Platinum has them); three to five (a horde's wild side) stand on an arc across the platform, its ends 0.9 of the radius out and level with the middle's spot, its middle half the radius further from the other side, the first at the end the first of two stands on (the foe's right) and the rest in order across. They are scaled 0.6 for three, 0.52 for four and 0.45 for five, so a horde's five stand within the platform's own circle, a little over a tile apart, the back ones seen over the front ones from the overview: the overview's lens is not widened for them, since the platform is in it already. A move that hits all five is framed as any move on several (`BattleCamera`: the shot of their middle wide enough for the spread, its lens up to `WidestFov`), and five sent out together the same.
- **An ally answers** (plan 14 · B5, `BattleAnimator.Arrive`, `Graphics/BattlePlaces.cs`'s `BattleAllies`): an SOS battle's wild Pokémon stands alone at its platform's middle, whole, while its side's second place is empty (`BattleAnimator.Gather`: the side's spread is 0). The caller hops where it stands and cries as its call is read. An ally that answers comes from behind its place, away from the other side, 0.4 of its sprite frame's width back, in one arc 0.25 of that width high at its top, a little smaller at first (0.85, eased to whole), and lands at 0.7 s (`ArriveTime`), crying as it comes down; meanwhile the side opens out to a double's two places over the same 0.7 s (`BattlePlaces.Spot(side, slot, slots, spread)` and `Fit(slots, spread)`, the double's own to the bit once opened), the caller sliding to the right and shrinking to 0.78, and stays opened out for the rest of the battle. Its HP box slides in as it hops. With the battle scene off it is simply there, and the side open.
- **A triple's places** (plan 14 · B6, `Graphics/BattlePlaces.cs`): the foe's three stand on the arc of three a horde's three would (ends 0.9 of the radius out, the middle half the radius further back), and the player's own three stand in a straight row across their platform, the ends 0.9 of the radius out and the middle on the platform's spot, so that seen from behind none stands in front of another; all six at 0.6. The player's first stands on the left as a double's first does and the foe's first on the right, so each of the player's faces the foe the rules call across from it (the player's first and the foe's third on the left). The overview frames both platforms as it is; three sent out together are framed as a group, as a horde's are.
- **A Battle Royal's ring** (plan 14, finishing B13; `Graphics/BattlePlaces.cs`, `RingSpots`, `ArenaSpec.Ring`): the four camps stand on four platforms round the field's middle, each alone on its own, and from the overview they read left to right as the player, the third, the second and the first: the player's where it always is; the first on the arena's own platform, where a single's foe stands; the second on a platform of the ring's own (radius 1.5, of the arena's own make: the same top and rim, laid by the arena's builder with the foe's) at (−3.6, −3), further back and in the middle of the picture; the third on another at (−6.6, 4), nearer the lens, toward the player's left and clear of the player's own Pokémon. No platform stands in front of another from the overview. The three are scaled 0.65 (`RingFit`), a little under a single's size; each turns half way toward the ring's middle (the four spots taken together, `RingMiddle`), which is nothing for the first. Their trainers stand on their own platforms as the battle opens, turned the same way, and step off to their sides. Nothing of it reaches any other format: a Royal's stage is a stage of its own (`ArenaSpec` with `Ring`), and every other battle's places, stage and shots are what they were. The overview frames all four as it is; the quarters of a manner that splits a Royal (`CameraSplits.RoyalQuarters`) are framed on these places.
- **A rotation's turntable** (plan 14 · B7, `Graphics/RotationFx.cs`): in a rotation battle each platform is a turntable with one Pokémon at its middle, whole, as a single's. On the platform lies a ring 0.82 of its radius out (cool white `206,218,236` at 150 alpha) with three six-sided seats on it a third of a round apart, each 0.13 of the radius, the front one (toward the other side) lit gold (`255,214,96`) with a soft glow and the two behind slate (`150,164,190`), all lying flat in the scene (depth-tested, alpha-blended). A turn takes 0.7 s (`BattleAnimator.RotationTime`, eased in and out): the seats go round a third, the front's gold passing to the one coming forward, two pale arrows lie on the rim either side of the front pointing the way it turns and fade in and out over the turn, and the Pokémon brought forward hops onto the middle as an answering ally hops in (from behind, 0.85 growing to whole), the one it replaces gone at once. The Pokémon behind are not drawn on the stage: the seats and the commands (`"Battle panels"`, "The rotation arrows") tell who sits there. With the battle scene off the table stands turned and the Pokémon is simply there.
- **A totem's aura** (plan 23 · Z6, `Graphics/MoveFx.Totem.cs`, `CombatantView.TotemAge`): our own, since Sun and Moon's is a glowing outline the shader here doesn't draw. From the line that wraps the totem in its aura (`TotemAuraRaised`) to the battle's end: six wisps of gold flame (`255,196,64`, every third ember `236,112,40`) rise up the edges of its body by turns, each 1.6 s on its way, leaning in as they climb and fading in and out (alpha-blended, 0.17 h shrinking to 0.12 h, each with a halo `255,226,150` added at 45 %); an ember glow lies flat under its feet at half strength, `w + 0.25 h` across; a glint over its head once in 2.2 s. It comes up over 0.5 s, stands back as the lasting marks do (`BattleAnimator.LastingShown`), stays with the Battle Scene off, and draws at most 14 quads. The totem is drawn at its kind's own size (Sun and Moon's are bigger).
- Sky (`SkyPainter`): zenith `78,146,226`, middle `140,194,244` at 22 % height, hazy horizon `224,238,250` at 46 %; soft cumulus with a white crown and a cool `196,212,236` underside, drifting slowly.

### Arenas (G8)

The stage depends on where the battle starts (`ArenaSpec`, `BattleArenas`): outdoors the ground under the player picks it, and a map can name its own (data-files.md). Every arena keeps the same layout (platforms, camera) and the smooth style: vertex gradients and soft textures, modelled foliage and rock, no texture noise.
