| Arena | Ground and platforms | Around the field | Light (`ArtLook.ArenaRig`) | In the air (`ArenaFx`) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Grass (routes, towns) | meadow, grass tops on dirt rims | the map's trees, hills; a lake if the map has one | the time of day's battle rig | fireflies at night |
| Forest | darker meadow with leaf litter, mossy tops | five rows of trees, trees closing in at both sides, bushes, ferns, fallen logs, mushrooms | battle rig × 0.8 sun, green shade and haze (fog +15 %), vignette +0.16; after dark only a dark green haze, so the Pokémon stay clear | slanting light shafts and pollen by day, fireflies at night |
| Cave | warm grey-brown rock floor, flat stone tops | rock walls all round (lumpy masses, dark at the foot, lit on top), stalagmites, boulders, blue and violet crystals that glow | fixed: torch-warm key from the upper left `0.58,0.45,0.31`, cool blue shade, dark haze (55 %), vignette 0.42 | dim warm dust |
| Water | open water to the horizon | the platforms are rocky islets with sandy tops and foam rings; islands and sea stacks far off | the time of day's battle rig | sun glints on the water |
| Snow | white-blue snowfield, packed-snow tops | pines laden with snow, drifts, capped rocks, white mountains | the battle rig with a paler overcast sky, light bounced up off the ground (ground ambient × 1.3), saturation × 0.88, contrast 1.0 | falling snow |
| Sand | rippled sand, sandstone tops | dunes, banded mesas, dry shrubs, bleached rocks | the battle rig, hotter and hazier: warm sun, warm fog, more bloom | sand blowing across |
| City (paving where the area's header names a city's background, plan 22, wave 48) | flagstones `222,216,200` / `210,204,186` with grout `170,164,150`; platform tops of paving `234,228,212` to `214,206,188` with two worn rings, stone rims `150,146,140` | a row of buildings behind the field, 7 to 13 units wide and 8 to 18 tall, in cream, brick, slate, coral and stone, each with a darker roof edge and plinth and rows of windows that light up after dark (the stage's light pass); four lamps along the kerb behind the field, trees in round stone planters at both sides, two benches | the time of day's battle rig | none |
| Underwater (the sea floor, plan 21 · H5) | sea sand `196,190,150` with paler patches `214,206,166` and darker `170,166,128`; the platforms are low rock under sandy tops (`206,196,154` to `164,154,120`), a rim of `104,108,100` | rows of kelp behind the field and closing in at both sides, each stalk tapering from `44,82,64` at its holdfast to `132,170,104` at its tip with a blade off it every so often, swaying; masses of rock (`52,70,78` at the foot to `124,140,132` lit), boulders on the sand; the sea all round, no horizon | fixed, as a cave's: a blue-green key from nearly overhead `0.52,0.68,0.66`, blue all round `0.40,0.52,0.64`, warmth bounced off the sand `0.36,0.40,0.34`; a blue fog `0.10,0.30,0.38` taking 0.62 of the colour from 16 to 64 units; the sky a sea from `92,170,184` overhead to `20,56,80` at its foot, no clouds, no stars; saturation 0.82, shade leaning blue, bloom only over 0.88 | bubbles rising in threes and alone (each a body under its rim), slanting shafts of blue-green light, pale motes drifting |
| Sky (a sky battle's, plan 14 · B2, wherever it is met) | no ground: a sea of cloud nine units below the platforms (`236,240,248` / `250,251,255`, shade `214,222,238`); the platforms are heaps of cloud, nine puffs round each (`196,208,230` in their folds to `244,247,253` on top) under flat white tops (`252,253,255` to `234,240,250`, edge `206,218,238`) with a rim of `214,224,242` | puffs of cloud in threes at the sides of the field and behind it, 2.4 to 6 units across, `204,214,234` to `248,250,255`, none within six units of a platform | the time of day's battle rig, under the open sky | wisps of cloud blowing across from the left, white by day and a greyed blue after dark |
| Indoors | floorboards, round rugs | panelled walls with daylit windows, bookshelves, potted plants | the room's light of the time of day | dust in the light |
| Gym (per type) | polished tiles in the type's tones, the type's emblem between the platforms, raised drums ringed with light | walls with banners in the type's colour, columns with lamps, the type's props (boulders, planters, pools, candles, girders, ice crystals, tesla coils, braziers, training posts) | fixed: neutral key, ambient leaning 16 % toward the type, bloom 0.32 | the type's matter (embers, bubbles, leaves, sparks, snow, wisps, motes) |
| League (Aaron, Bertha, Flint, Lucian; the Champion) | as a gym, darker and grander; the Champion's room white and gold | as a gym | as a gym, ambient × 0.78, bloom 0.45, vignette 0.38 | as a gym, more of it |

Walls, rock faces and rooms take shadows but cast none, so they never darken the platforms. Lamps, crystals and glowing rims are added light (the stage's light pass).

### Camera (G8)

`BattleCamera` directs the shots from the battle's state alone; the overview is the framing above (9° pitch, 24° field of view).

- **Intro**: the sweep in from the side (0.55 rad round and 35 % further out, easing into the overview over 1.8 s).
- **Send-out**: a closer shot of the platform a Pokémon is thrown onto, until it has come out (3.6 × its height in frame).
- **The foe's shots stand in front of the player's platform** (2.5 units or more): a foe too big to frame from there gets a wider lens (up to 40°) rather than a camera among the player's trainer and Pokémon.
- **A move**: the attacker as it winds up (3 × its height in frame; the player's Pokémon over its left shoulder with the foe beyond, the foe from the front), then a **cut** to the target 0.24 s in, just before the impact (3 × its height; two targets are framed together), back to the overview by 0.95 s. A status move on the user stays on the user.
- **Critical hit**: a cut punch-in to 2.1 × the target's height at the impact, for 0.4 s.
- **Thrown ball**: over to the trainer running in; from behind them, turning to where the ball is going and zooming in (to 15° at the narrowest, so the arc stays in frame) as it flies; a cut to the ball opening over the foe; then close on the ball on the platform (2 units in frame) while it wobbles and clicks.
- **Faint**: toward the fainting Pokémon.
- **A turntable turning** (plan 14 · B7): while a rotation battle's turntable turns (0.7 s), a shot of that side's platform as a send-out's, a little wider (4.4 × the height in frame, `BattleCamera.RotationShotScale`) so the seats on the rim are in it; after a send-out's shot and before a faint's.
- **A trainer who speaks** (plan 11 · C13): while a trainer's line is read (a "Battle panels" pill over it), the camera eases (0.18 s, as any shot) onto a shot of them beside their Pokémon: from the front as the foe's shots are (0.18 rad round, a little more pitch), 2.2 × the trainer's height in frame, centred three fifths of the way from their Pokémon to them and 0.55 of their height above the platform, so their feet stand clear of the message box over the picture's lowest fifth. Once the line is read the camera eases back as they step off again. It wins over a move's shots and a send-out's, which a line is read after; while the player chooses a command it is the overview, as always.
- **A signature move** (plan 25 · Y17; `Graphics/Direction.cs`, "Signature moves" under "Move effects (G8)") takes one of four shots of its own in place of the move's, each a function of the cue's shown age, held to 1.15 s (`Directions.ShotEnd`) instead of 0.95 so its pull-back is seen, and eased back to the overview after:
  - *Charge-up push*: the attacker's shot pushing in from 3 to 2.2 × its height over the wind-up, then a **cut** back to the target at 0.24 s (the snap of the release); on a charge turn (Solar Beam's first) the push runs from 3 to 2.1 over the whole charge.
  - *Follow the projectile*: from the start to the impact the camera looks at the point where the beam or missile is, its frame growing from the user's size to the target's (3.4 × the height). Sent by the player's own Pokémon, the camera stays over its shoulder (the attacker's shot) and turns after the shot, narrowing on it; sent by a foe, it rides alongside from the overview's side turned 0.6 rad and lifted 0.12, never further south than 3.2 units before the player's platform (`FollowLimit`), so it never crosses the player's side. Then the target's shot.
  - *Impact punch-in*: the attacker, the target, a **cut** in to 2.0 × the target's height at the impact, held through the hit-stop and 0.4 s after, then a late pull-back out to 3.8 × by 1.15 s. A blow whose mark is on the ground (the quakes' ring of cracks, Sacred Sword's blades: `MoveDirection.Low`, plan 25 · Y17g) is punched in on looking 0.3 of the target's height lower, camera and look point moved down together, so the feet and what lies before them are in the shot from the impact on.
  - *Orbit*: the attacker, then the target framed at 3.6 × swung round it and back (a sine out and home, 1.8 rad at the furthest round the foe, 1.2 rad round the player's own Pokémon, always the way away from the trainer), so the move's spread is seen from the side.
  - **The motion number**: every one of these is laid over the move's ordinary shots by `BattleCamera.Motion` (1; 0 under plan 12 · Q9's reduce-motion option, where the ordinary shots play), and the hit-stop by the same number. None of it plays with the Battle Scene off (no cue plays), and a signature move on its user alone keeps the user's shot.
- **A giant's growth** (plan 25 · YD1; `ShotKind.Giant`): while a Dynamax or its end plays (to 0.3 s before its end, then eased back to the overview), the newest of it and a move's cue taking the camera. Through a trainer's call: the foe's trainer and their platform from the front as a speaker's shot (a frame of 2.6 × the trainer's height between them and their Pokémon), or the player's own platform from its right as a send-out's, a little wider (4.2 × the height). Through the growth: **low, looking up the body**: the camera stands where a frame of 1.7 × the grown height (the body at its full Dynamax size) would put it seen from the overview's side (the foe's at the foe's shot's turn, the player's at its own, from behind its right shoulder, turned 0.75 rad, `GiantOwnYaw`; in a battle of two or more a side an end place's turned 1.5 rad to its outer side instead, `PairOuterYaw`, the first to the west and the last to the east, side-on with the partner behind the giant and further off: from the right shoulder the first's partner stood as near as the giant across the picture's middle) pitched 5° below level, so it looks up; its target is 0.45 of the body's height above the feet as it stands, so the feet stand clear of the message box over the picture's lowest fifth, and its lens widens as the body grows (the frame 1.7 × the body as it stands, 14° at the narrowest and `WidestFov` 40° at the widest). The foe's stays in front of `FoeShotLimit` as every foe's shot does. It eases in from the overview and back to it; with `BattleCamera.Motion` under 1 it is laid over the overview by that share (none of it at nought).
- **A Mega Dynamax** (plan 25 · YD4): the Mega Evolution of a Mega Dynamax (the batch's lines tell of a `MegaEvolved` and, later, a `Dynamaxed` into a form of `FormKind.MegaDynamax` at the same place) takes the giant shot through its light, framed for the body it will grow to, and holds it once the light is over (`BattleAnimator.MegaMaxHold`, `BattleCamera.MegaMaxHeld`) until its growth begins, so the overview never comes between the two halves; a move's shots still take the camera in between (another Pokémon moving first), and the hold is let go by the growth, a faint or a recall there, and a menu. A plain Mega Evolution keeps its own shots.
- **A Max Move** (plan 25 · YD2; `ShotKind.MaxMove`, `BattleCamera.MaxMoveShot`), read at the cue's shown age, for every Max Move and G-Max Move that is not the user's own (Max Guard keeps the ordinary self shot): *the user from below* (0 to 0.24 s: its body framed at 3.2 × its height from its own side, the foe's at the foe's shot's turn and the player's from its left, turned 0.55 rad east of the overview's side (`MaxOwnYaw`), away from the platform's west where its trainer stands to call a Dynamax in the same turn; in a battle of two or more a side an end place's turned to its outer side as its giant shot is (`BattleCamera.PairYaw`), the partner behind it, pitched 4° below level and never lower than a quarter unit over its feet); *the whip* (0.24 to 0.32 s, an ease of 0.08 s, never a cut): the camera stays where it stood and swings its aim across the stage onto the target, its lens narrowing or widening to fit the target at 3.4 × its height (12° to `WidestFov`); held on the target to the impact. From the foe's side onto the player's own there is no whip: the player's platform is too near the foe's shot to look at and an ease would pass it, so it cuts at 0.24 s to the player's Pokémon at 3.4 × as an ordinary move's attacker cuts to its target. A shot of the player's own never frames less than 2.2 units (`MaxOwnLeast`), so a small body's punch-in keeps the camera clear of its platform; *the punch-in* (a landed one, from the impact): the target at 2.1 × its height, as a critical hit's, held 0.4 s of shown age, which the hit-stop makes 0.52 s of the clock; *the pull-back*: eased out over 0.5 s to 4.6 × the target's height, wide enough to hold the set piece, and held to 1.4 s, when it lets go and eases (or cuts, from the foe's side) back to the overview. A miss is held on the target at 3.4 × to 0.95 s. Within the shot the camera follows its own function frame by frame (the move from the whip's aim to the punch-in is the one cut, as a critical's); into it and out of it it eases as any shot. Every position stands clear of the player's platform by `Clearance` and never passes it between frames. Laid over the ordinary move's shots by `BattleCamera.Motion` (none of it at nought: the attacker, the cut, back by 0.95 s).
- Shots ease into each other (time constant 0.18 s); only attacker-to-target and the critical punch-in cut, and any move that would carry the camera across the player's platform (from behind it to in front of it, or back) or within 3 units of it, where the trainer or the player's Pokémon would loom up in front of the lens. While the player chooses a command the camera is on the overview under Classic; a manner may film the menu (below, "The battle's manners", "The idle cycle"), and the player's own front is only ever cut to and from. Hits shake it (more for critical and super-effective ones; Earthquake and Hyper Beam add their own).
- **Reduce motion** (plan 12 · Q9, the options' REDUCE MOTION): every move of the camera beyond its plain shots hangs on one number, `BattleCamera.Motion`, 1 normally and nought with the row on. At nought nothing shakes (a hit's, Earthquake's, any effect's) and a critical hit is watched from the ordinary target shot, with no punch-in; the shots, their cuts and their eases are the same. Whatever later moves the camera more (plan 25 · Y17's signature shots and hit-stop, the Dynamax plan's whip, punch-in and pull-back) scales by the same number.
- **A held shot: photo mode in battle** (plan 12 · Q17; `Graphics/BattlePhoto.cs`, `GameEngine.BattlePhoto.cs`; `ShotKind.Held`): the Photo action (F9, the pad's Select) at any moment of a battle **stills it**: the battle is not updated while a photo is composed, so the animator's clock, every cue, the camera's ease, the effects, the weather's fall and the HP bars stand where they were, and nothing reaches the rules (no choice is handed over, no line is read on, a recorded battle replays the same). The director is held (`BattleCamera.Hold`): it eases nowhere, shakes nothing, and the shot shown is the photo's, which starts as the shot the director had on screen (whatever it was: the overview, an attacker, a ball, a giant) and is turned from there. The arrows orbit it round the point that shot looked at (a Pokémon, a trainer, a ball), or, for a shot that looks near the field's middle (the overview, whose target lies 20 units ahead under the ground), round the point of its line nearest the middle (a unit over the ground halfway between the platforms), Left and Right round the vertical at 1.2 rad a second (any way round, behind the player's platform included), Up and Down raise or lower it at 0.6 rad a second between 2° and 70° of pitch, never under 0.4 units above the ground; the page buttons bring it closer or further a step of × 1.25 between 0.6 and 2.5 times as close as it began; its lens is the shot's own. **The panel** is the field's (same place, size and look), titled PHOTO, its rows ZOOM, TIME, POSE, FILTER and FRAME: TIME borrows a period's hour for the stage's light (the middle of the period, as in the field); POSE steps the Pokémon's own idle and blink a tenth of a second at a time from where they stood (AS IT IS, then +0.1 s to +2.9 s, round again), a pose being a function of the animator's time and the step alone, while everything else stays still; FILTER and FRAME are the field's, the filter laid last over the arena's rig and the Cinema frame drawn as the kit's bars (120, black) over the stage. There is no weather row: a battle's weather is the rules'. **The interface is hidden over the stage**: the HP boxes, the menus, the message box and the captions are not drawn while a photo is composed, only the stage, its frame and (unless Item hides them or the picture is being taken) the panel and the hint. A takes the picture through the screenshot key's own capture; B shuts the panel, or with it shut lets the director go, which eases back from the held shot to whatever it wants as the battle goes on.

### The battle's manners (plan 45)

How a battle is filmed beyond the director's plain shots (above) is a **manner**: a row of `CameraManners` (`Graphics/CameraManner.cs`, GPU-free), each column a number or a switch that does nothing at nought or off. A region's file names its manner (`battleCamera`; a region that names none is filmed as Sinnoh is), and the options' BATTLE CAMERA row (on the BATTLE tab) chooses: **CLASSIC** (the director as it was before plan 45, to the bit), **REGION** (each region's own manner; the default) or **LIVELY** (Galar's everywhere). REDUCE MOTION (`BattleCamera.Motion` at nought) films every manner as Classic. Every number here is ours, judged on the harness's pictures; nothing is measured off any game's footage. A manner is the face's alone: nothing of it reaches the rules, the log or a recorded battle, and every shot it gives is a function of the animator's clock, the menu's open time and the cue's seed.

The columns (the session that turns each on in brackets):

| Column | What it does | Off |
|---|---|---|
| `Drift`, `DriftPeriod` | the menu's breathing overview: a drift round the field either way (radians) over a breath of so many seconds of the menu's open time, never off the overview's side (BC2) | 0 |
| `Cycle`, `CycleHold`, `CycleEase` | the idle cycle: the shots the menu is filmed with in turn (`IdleShot`), each held so long and eased into the next over so long (BC2) | none |
| `Reach`, `Low` | how far the cycle's shots reach (a share) and how much lower they stand (radians of pitch) (BC6) | 1, 0 |
| `Push` | a move's shots pushed in on the user and pulled out past the target, a share of Galar's (BC4) | 0 |
| `React` | a short shot of the one hit after a super-effective or critical hit or a faint (BC4) | off |
| `Open`, `Close`, `Crane` | the battle's own opening and closing shots, and the crane over a Leader's or a Champion's stage (BC5) | off |
| `Splits` | when the picture splits in two: the leads, every send-out, the last Pokémon (any trainer's, or a Leader's, an Elite Four's or a Champion's), both giants, a Terastallization, and whether a Battle Royal is split in quarters (BC3, BC7) | none |
| `Handheld` | how far the cycle's aim sways in the hand (radians), never a roll (BC6) | 0 |

The manners, from the quietest to the liveliest (a row is added with the session that builds it):

| Manner | Who | What it does |
|---|---|---|
| `classic` | the options' CLASSIC; every manner under REDUCE MOTION | nothing: every switch off |
| `kanto`, `johto`, `hoenn` | Kanto, Johto, Hoenn (BC6; games filmed flat) | the overview held at the menu with a slow push of its own lens in on the foe and back (`Cycle` overview, the foe zoom; `CycleHold` 6, 5.5 and 5 s, `CycleEase` 3.5, 3.5 and 3 s), the opening and the closing (`Open`, `Close`); no splits, no push on a move |
| `sinnoh` | Sinnoh, and any region whose file names none | Platinum's own seat: today's shots with a breathing overview at the menu (`Drift` 0.07 rad, `DriftPeriod` 18 s) and a split at a Leader's, an Elite Four's or a Champion's last Pokémon (`Splits` boss's last), and the battle's own opening and closing (`Open`, `Close`) |
| `unova` | Unova | a slow swing at the menu between the overview and over the shoulder (`Cycle` overview, shoulder; `CycleHold` 4.5 s, `CycleEase` 2.5 s) and a split at the leads (`Splits` leads); half Galar's push on a move's shots (`Push` 0.5); the opening and the closing (`Open`, `Close`) |
| `kalos` | Kalos (BC6; the first fully 3D generation) | the cycle through over the shoulder, the foe's face and the side (`CycleHold` 4 s, `CycleEase` 1.8 s), a move pushed in a little (`Push` 0.6) with its target's shot standing low as it is hit (`Low` 0.06 rad), the opening and the closing, and a split at the leads and at any trainer's last Pokémon |
| `alola` | Alola (BC6) | Kalos's, its cycle the side, over the shoulder and the foe's face, its shots wider (`Reach` 1.15) and lower (`Low` 0.12 rad), **with the trainers in them** (`TrainersIn`): once a trainer has sent their Pokémon out and run off, they walk back in over 0.8 s and stand by them for the rest of the battle: the player 1.6 west of their platform's edge and 0.6 behind its middle (`BattleRenderer.StandSpot`: wholly out of the overview, which only the side and shoulder shots see past, and on the far side from the side shot's camera, so they hide none of their Pokémon), walking in from the west and turned toward the foe; a trainer across the field where a speaker stands (`BattleStage.SpeakerSpot`) turned toward the player's side, the first walking in from the east and the second of two from the west. Neither stands there while they are back on their platform to speak or call, nor the player while running in to throw a ball or at a win, whose own walk in takes them |
| `galar` | the options' LIVELY, and Galar once its file is written | the camera never rests: the cycle through the overview, the side, over the shoulder, the foe's face, the crane (a Leader's, an Elite Four's or a Champion's battle alone) and the player's own front (`CycleHold` 3.5 s, `CycleEase` 1.4 s), and a split at the leads, at every send-out after them, at any trainer's last Pokémon and when both sides have a giant (`Splits` leads, send-outs, last Pokémon, giants); every move pushed in on its user and pulled out past its target (`Push` 1) and a reaction after a super-effective or critical blow (`React`); the opening and the closing, a boss's with the crane (`Open`, `Close`, `Crane`) |
| `hisui` | Hisui once its file is written (BC6; Legends: Arceus's battles were close and low) | Galar's cycle without the crane, nearer (`Reach` 0.85) and lower (`Low` 0.1 rad), its push (its target's shot lowered by `Low` too) and reactions, the opening and the closing; no splits |
| `paldea` | Paldea once its file is written (BC6) | Galar's, the cycle's aim held in the hand (`Handheld` 0.012 rad) and a split as a Pokémon Terastallizes (`Splits` Galar's and Terastallization) |

**The idle cycle** (BC2; `BattleCamera.IdleNow`, `Graphics/BattleCamera.Idle.cs`): while the commands or the move cards are open (`BattleAnimator.MenuAge`, the animator's own time since they opened; a target, BAG, POKéMON, a question or a line bring the overview back, and the next menu begins again from it), a manner that films the menu does so by its menu's open time, never by its frames:

- *Sinnoh's breathing overview* (`Drift`): the overview turned round the field's middle by `Drift` on a sine of `DriftPeriod` and coming 3.5 % closer at the top of each breath (`BreathZoom`), eased in from the plain overview over 1.5 s (`BreathIn`), so a menu opened and closed at once is the overview. It stays behind the player's platform, on the overview's side.
- *A cycle* (`Cycle`): its shots in turn, the overview first, each held `CycleHold` and eased into the next over `CycleEase` (smoothstep), round again. The shots (`IdleShot`), each framed for who stands where:
  - *the overview*, as ever;
  - *over the shoulder*: low behind the player's Pokémon (2.2 × its height back, at least a unit; its own width and 0.8 × its height to its right), the camera at 1.05 × its height, a 20° lens on a point 0.3 of the foe's height under the foes' middle, so the foes stand in the picture's upper middle and the player's Pokémon's back is the lower left's foreground; never nearer the player's platform than `Clearance` + 0.4;
  - *the foe's face*: the foes from the front, 2.6 × their height in a 26° lens (wider for a group, as a target shot), turned 0.28 rad from the foe's own shots, standing in front of the player's platform (`FoeShotLimit`);
  - *the player's own front* (`ShotKind.OwnFront`): the player's Pokémon from between the two platforms looking south at it, 2.8 × its height in a 26° lens, turned 0.28 rad and looking down 0.3 rad so the ground stands behind it;
  - *the side*: both sides from 22 units off the line between the platforms to the east, turned 0.45 rad toward the player's side (`SideTurn`) and lifted 0.16 rad, at the overview's lens, the two facing each other across the picture (the foes on the right).
  - *the crane* (BC5, a manner with `Crane`, a boss's battle alone): "Opening and closing", below;
  - *the foe zoom* (BC6, the flat manners'): the overview's own stand, its lens narrowed on the foes' middle until they fill 4.2 × their height (at least 6°), so the ease to and from the overview is a push of the lens alone and the camera never moves; where the player's own Pokémon would stand half in its lower left corner, narrowed 0.3 of the height at a time down to 2.4 until it is out of the picture, and left out of that battle's cycle where it never is.
- **Reach and low** (BC6): `Reach` multiplies how far every cycle shot stands (the shoulder's stand, the side's 22 units, the faces' and the split halves' frames), `Low` takes radians off their lift; with a push, `Low` also swings a move's target shot down round the target as it is hit (never under a quarter unit over the ground).
- **In the hand** (BC6, `Handheld`): the cycle's aim swayed across and up by up to that many radians on two sines each way of unrelated periods of the battle's clock (1.31 and 2.87 a second across, 1.07 and 2.41 up), eased in with the menu's open time over 1.5 s as the breath is; the camera stands where it stood and level, never a roll.
- **The picture's free part** (`IdleFrame`): an idle shot's subjects (the foes over the shoulder and in their face, the player's own in its front, everyone from the side) stand wholly inside a 24-unit margin of the 1920×1080 picture, above the commands, the move cards and the message box (y 836) and off the HP boxes of the battle's format (`IdleFrame.Panels`: a single's foe box at the top left, a double's two, a horde's or a triple's column; the player's box or boxes over the commands on the right). A shot whose subjects don't fit is widened a degree at a time up to `WidestFov` (40°), and left out of that battle's cycle where that doesn't do it. Whoever is not a shot's subject is out of its picture or in the free part too, but for the shoulder's foreground.
- **The corridors' rule**: the player's own front is reached by a cut and left by one, never eased (it stands on the far side of the player's Pokémon from every other shot); between any other two shots of the cycle the camera eases, unless the ease would pass the player's platform as the director's other eases never may (`CrossesPlayerSide`), where it cuts at the ease's middle. `BattleCamera.Update` cuts into and out of `ShotKind.OwnFront` whatever the shot before.
- **Leaving the cycle**: a move chosen, the camera eases (or cuts, where the old rule says) from wherever the cycle stood into the move's shots.
- Under REDUCE MOTION every manner films the menu from the overview, as Classic does.

**The split** (BC3; `BattleCamera.SplitNow`, `Graphics/BattleCamera.Split.cs`, `SplitBand`, `ModernUi.SplitBands`): at the moments a manner's `Splits` name, the picture is cut in two by a slanted band and each team is seen from the front, each half its own camera.

- **The band**: a slant from the picture's bottom to its top, leaning right, its middle line through the picture's middle from (760, 1080) to (1160, 0) of the 1920×1080 layout (about 20° from upright). 30 units wide: a rim of the frame colour (`ModernUi.Frame`) either side of an 18-unit white core (`PanelTop`) with a 4-unit line of the selection colour (`Selection`) down its middle, its ends round (`UiShapes.Line`). **Above it and to its left is the foe's half, below it and to its right the player's**, so the HP boxes, where they have always stood (the foe's at the top left, the player's at the bottom right), lie over their own halves and nothing of the interface moves. (Plan 45 drew the foe's half in the upper right; it was turned so the boxes keep their places.)
- **The halves**: the foe's is the foes from the front as the idle cycle's foe's face (`FoeFaceShot`'s stand and turn, a 26° lens, standing in front of the player's platform), framing 3.2 × their height (or 3.4 × a group's spread) and turned where it stands until their middle is seen at (560, 560), low in the upper left and clear of their HP box; the player's is their own from the front as the player's own front (`OwnFaceShot`'s stand between the platforms looking south), framing the same and turned until their middle is seen at (1420, 420), high in the lower right and clear of their box and the message box. Every Pokémon of a side stands wholly in its own half, a band's half-width clear of the band, off the HP boxes and above y 836 (`BattleCameraSplitTests`).
- **Coming in and going out**: never a cut. The two halves come in from their own corners, each bounded by an edge parallel to the band with a band along it, over 0.3 s (`SplitIn`, smoothstep), the director's own shot showing between them until the two edges meet at the band's middle; held; then they go back out to their corners the same way over 0.3 s (`SplitOut`) onto whatever the director's shot is then. Held 1.4 s at the leads (`SplitHoldLeads`) and 1.6 s at a last Pokémon (`SplitHoldLast`). Anything that waits on the player (the commands, the move cards, a question, the switch offer) or a trainer stepping back to speak cuts it short: it goes out from that moment, or from when it has come all the way in, whichever is later, and never comes back.
- **When** (`CameraSplits`; the animator notes the moments whatever the manner, `BattleAnimator.SplitMoments`): *the leads*, once every place of both sides is filled and nobody is still coming out of a ball, after the opening's sweep: in a trainer's battle, and in a wild battle only against a legendary or mythical Pokémon (decision 3); *a last Pokémon*, once a trainer's last is out of its ball (the core's `TrainerReacts` of `LastPokemon`, whatever the options say of trainers' lines); *a boss's last*, the same of a Gym Leader, an Elite Four or a Champion. Never with the Battle Scene off, never under REDUCE MOTION, never while a photo holds the shot.
- **How it is drawn**: one shadow map for the frame; the director's own view into the scene target as ever while the halves come in or go out (not drawn while they cover the picture); each half into a target of its own the scene target's shape and at most 1920×1080 (made while a split is on screen and let go after), drawn only within its half's bounding box (a scissor); each half laid over its own part of the picture through the composite's own grading (its tilt, outlines, tints, saturation, contrast and vignette; the scene's blur, glow and occlusion, which are made from the scene target, are not laid on a half); the band over their edges; the HP boxes and the menus over all as ever. Held whole, each half's box is 60 % of its picture, so the two draw 2.5 million pixels in their 1080p targets, against the scene target's 8.3 million on High and 4.7 million on Medium; on Low (a 1080p scene) the halves' targets are made smaller (91 %) so that a split held draws no more than the scene. While the halves come in or go out (0.3 s each) the director's own view is drawn beside them.
- **The later moments** (BC6): *a send-out*, a Pokémon of either side of a trainer's battle out of its ball after the leads' moment, held 1.1 s; *both giants*, the frame both sides first each have a Dynamaxed Pokémon grown, held 1.6 s (once each time it comes about); *a Terastallization*, once its shell has shattered (1.7 s after it begins), held 1.4 s. A last Pokémon's moment, which comes in the same frame as its send-out's, is the newer and wins.
- The manners' rows: Sinnoh's splits at a boss's last; Unova's at the leads; Kalos's and Alola's at the leads and at any trainer's last Pokémon; Galar's at the leads, every send-out, any trainer's last Pokémon and both giants; Paldea's at Galar's and a Terastallization; Kanto's, Johto's, Hoenn's and Hisui's never. A Battle Royal is split in quarters by Kalos's, Alola's, Galar's and Paldea's ("Formats and the rest", below) and by no other.

**Push in, pull out** (BC4; `BattleCamera.PushedAttacker`, `PulledTarget`, `Graphics/BattleCamera.Push.cs`): a manner's `Push` (a share of Galar's whole push, 1; times `Motion`) moves an ordinary move's two shots. *The attacker's* dollies in over the wind-up, from 3 × its height in frame to 3 − 0.6 × the push (2.4 at a whole push), smoothstep, to the cut. *The target's* starts tight at the cut (its usual 3, or a status move's 3.4, less 0.4 × the push), is held so to the impact (0.35 s; 0.25 s longer for a heavy blow, one with a hit-stop in `Directions`), then pulls out to the usual plus 0.6 × the push (3.6 at a whole push) by 0.95 s, smoothstep, so the move's set piece is in the picture. A critical hit's punch-in is as ever. A signature move's shots and a Max Move's win over these as they win over the plain ones (the push moves only what they are laid over). Every pushed shot stands `Clearance` + 0.25 clear of the player's platform: where a frame would bring the camera nearer, it is framed a tenth of a height wider at a time until it doesn't (`BattleCamera.Clear`).

**Reactions** (BC4; `BattleCamera.ReactNow`, `ShotKind.React`): with a manner's `React` on, once a super-effective or critical blow's own shots are over (0.95 s into its cue; a signature move's at 1.15 s, a Max Move's at its let-go, 1.4 s) the one it landed on is held close for 0.6 s (`ReactTime`): 2.5 × its height in frame, the foe from the front turned 0.12 rad more than its own shots, the player's own from its right as a send-out's, clear of the platform as a pushed shot. Never once another move has begun (its shots have the camera at once), never at a menu (the menu's shot has it), never on one fainting, gone or out of sight: a faint keeps its own shot, which is already the one hit's. The battle reads none of it, so no line is read a moment later for a reaction (`BattleCameraPushTests.NoLineIsReadALaterMomentForTheCamera`). The animator notes the newest such blow (`BattleAnimator.LastStrike`) whatever the manner. In a trainer's battle the trainer is not filmed (they have stepped off their platform; BC5's closing is the first face-on shot of a trainer).
- The manners' rows: Unova's pushes half Galar's (`Push` 0.5) and doesn't react; Galar's pushes whole and reacts.

**Opening and closing** (BC5; `BattleCamera.OpeningNow`, `ClosingNow`, `Graphics/BattleCamera.Open.cs`; `ShotKind.Opening`, `Closing`): a manner's `Open` takes the place of the sweep (which stays Classic's own) and its `Close` films the battle's end. Both are laid under every shot the battle has of its own (a send-out, a move, a ball, a faint, a trainer who speaks, a giant), follow their own functions frame by frame, and hold no line.

- **A wild opening**: from the overview's own stand, the lens narrowed onto the foes (2.6 × their height in frame, at least a 6° lens) and held 0.5 s, then widened and turned back to the overview by 1.8 s (smoothstep). The camera itself never moves, so nothing passes the player's platform, on which the player still stands.
- **A trainer's opening**: while the trainer (or two) still stands on their platform with nothing of theirs out, their whole height from the front as a speaker is (0.18 rad round, lifted 0.06), framing 1.9 × their height and pushing in to 1.6 × over 3 s (1.35 × wider for two), standing in front of the player's platform (`FoeShotLimit`). Their throw's send-out shot follows as ever, then the player's. Once nobody stands to be filmed the camera is on the overview: a manner that opens never plays the sweep.
- **The crane** (`Crane`, Galar's; a Gym Leader's, an Elite Four's or a Champion's battle, `BattleAnimator.BossBattle`, the first trainer's class): high over the stage, 30 units from the field's middle to the east of the line between the platforms, pitched 0.95 rad (54°) and looking at the middle at a 34° lens, both sides and the stage's ground in the picture; it stands north of the player's platform, so its eases with the foe's shots never pass it. At a boss's opening the trainer's shot comes down out of it over 2.6 s (smoothstep); at a win it rises out of the win's shot after 2.6 s, over 2.6 s; in the idle cycle it is a shot between the foe's face and the player's own front, at a boss's battle alone (left out of every other).
- **A win** (`BattleClosing.Won`, noted by the face as the log's `Won` is shown, with the Battle Scene on): the first face-on shot of the player's trainer. The player walks back in from 4.5 units west behind their platform over 0.8 s (eased to a stop) to a spot 1.05 east and 1.25 south of it (`WinnerSpot`, so a big Pokémon doesn't hide them), turns to the field over 0.25 s and cheers; the camera, **cut to** (it stands on the far side of the player's Pokémon from every other shot), stands between the platforms looking south at the player's standing Pokémon and the trainer together, turned 0.28 rad as the player's own front and looking down 0.24 rad at a 28° lens, framing 2.4 × the tallest of them and pushing in by 8 % over 4 s; widened a degree at a time up to `WidestFov` until everyone stands in the picture's free part (`IdleFrame`). Nobody walks back in under Classic or REDUCE MOTION, whose camera never looks.
- **The fill**: the battle's sun stands behind the player's platform, so a face turned to a shot of the player's own side is in its own shade. The player's own front (the idle cycle's) and the win's shot take a fill from the camera's side (`ArtLook.FaceFill`): the ground's ambient raised by 0.3 of the sun's colour and the sky's by 0.1; no other shot's light changes.
- **A loss**: the foes standing, cheering, from the front, framing 3 × their height at the idle lens (26°), turned half the foe's face's turn; fitted to the free part as an idle shot.
- **A catch** keeps the ball's own shots, as ever.
- The closing comes once the shots of the end (the last faint, a trainer's line) are over and lasts until the battle ends, which it ends exactly where it always did: nothing of it is waited for. A question after the end (a move to learn) keeps it on screen.
- The manners' rows: every manner but Classic opens and closes; Galar's and Paldea's with the crane.

**Formats and the rest** (BC7; `Graphics/BattleCamera.Royal.cs`, `CameraSplits.RoyalQuarters`, `ShotKind.Ally`, `BattleCamera.PhotoStart`): what a battle's format and the options change in the filming. None of it reaches the rules, the log or a recorded battle; every shot is still a function of the animator's clock.

- **Who is in a shot is who stands there.** Every cycle shot and split half frames the places of a side that are *present*: an SOS battle's wild side before its ally has landed, a double's place whose Pokémon has gone, is not a subject (a side with nobody present frames its first place, as before). A **double**, a **triple** and a **horde** are framed as the group they are (their middle, their widest spread and their tallest, as the idle cycle's faces have always done), so a triple's split halves and cycle shots hold all three of a side, a horde's up to five, wholly in the picture's free part or are widened to `WidestFov` or left out. A **rotation** battle is one place a side: its camera frames the front seat's Pokémon alone, the two seats behind it being on the table and not in the battle, and the turntable's turn keeps its own shot (above).
- **An ally hopping in** (an SOS battle, `ShotKind.Ally`, a manner with `React`): while an ally lands on its place (0.7 s, `BattleAnimator.ArriveTime`), the foes' side from the front at a send-out's framing, so the second Pokémon is seen arriving and the picture does not rest on the overview over an empty place; it follows a send-out's and a rotation's shot and precedes a faint's, and gives way to a move as a reaction does. A rotation battle's front seat hops on the same way and keeps the rotation's shot instead.
- **The Battle Royal's quarters** (`CameraSplits.RoyalQuarters`): four camps, each for itself, so a manner that splits and has the flag films the leads, a last Pokémon or a Terastallization of a Royal with the picture cut in **four upright quarters** of 480 layout units, left to right the three foes (by their places) and then the player, each from the front as the halves' foe is (the foe's face's stand, a 26° lens widened to `WidestFov` until its Pokémon stands wholly inside its quarter, a band's half-width clear of the quarter's edges and off the HP boxes and above the menu, at that quarter's own anchor). A quarter comes in from its own middle line over 0.3 s (the director's own shot showing between them) and goes out the same way, with a band along each edge (the halves' band, upright). The two targets of the halves are all it needs: quarters one and three go into the foe's, two and four into the player's (their boxes never overlap), each only within its own box, so the four draw one picture of pixels and no more than the scene does. A Royal with fewer than three foes standing, or a manner without the flag (Sinnoh's, Unova's, Hisui's, the flat ones), is not split in quarters: with the flag missing it isn't split at all, with a camp gone it is split in halves as any battle is. Kalos's, Alola's, Galar's and Paldea's have the flag.
- **A Frontier Brain is a boss** (`BattleMeeting.IsBoss`: a Gym Leader, an Elite Four, a Champion or a Frontier Brain, the Tower Tycoon, the Hall Matron, the Factory Head, the Arcade Star and the Castle Valet): their battle is filmed with the crane and their last Pokémon with a boss's split. The Frontier's rooms (the Tower, the Factory, the Hall, the Castle and the Arcade are fought on the Gym's hall, the League's, the Subway's train on the City's street) are walled: no shot of any manner stands outside a hall's walls, whatever the stage (`BattleStage.Enclosure`, which the hall's and the room's walls are drawn from). The Battle Palace has no commands, so its cycle never runs (it has opening, closing, pushes, reactions and splits); a Frontier battle's RUN question and the Arena's judge's card are the interface's, as ever.
- **A photo composed over the cycle** (`BattleCamera.PhotoStart`): the photo's held shot begins where the camera stands as the key is pressed: an idle shot of the cycle (the shoulder, the foe's face, the player's own front, the side, the crane, the foe zoom, the breathing overview, in the hand's sway) is held as it is, the lens of the shot and not the overview's; during a split it begins from the foe's half (the first quarter of a Royal), the half the picture shows at the top. The arrows turn it round what it looks at as ever (`BattlePhotoView`); letting go of the photo, the cycle resumes from the overview as a menu opened afresh does.
- **A link battle is filmed on each screen by its own options**: each player's screen reads its own BATTLE CAMERA row and its own REDUCE MOTION, from its own side (side B's view is turned round, so each films their own Pokémon as the player's side), at the same turn of the same log; one may film in Galar's manner and the other as Classic, and what the two see is the same battle line for line.
- **A recorded battle watched** (the Vs. Recorder's file) is filmed by the watcher's options. It has no commands, so it runs no cycle; it has its opening, closing, pushes, reactions and splits, and the lines stand and go on as they always have.
- **The options' edge cases**: REDUCE MOTION films every manner as Classic, in a photo, a link battle and a recording watched too. A Battle Scene turned off films as Classic as well (the scene off has no camera moves of its own: no cycle, no push, no reaction, no opening, no closing, no split, as the splits already had it). A split under REDUCE FLASHES has no flash to hold down (the band is steady). A manner never changes the number of the lines read, the rolls or the pace.

### Move effects (G8)

- **One kit** (`BattleFx`): streams of particles from the user to the target, beams, forked bolts, bursts thrown from the impact, shockwaves (rings growing on the ground or facing the camera), auras (rings and motes rising round the user), rain from above, a contact flash, slashes, orbs, waves, glints and screen flashes. Every effect is a function of its cue's age and seed: nothing is simulated.
- **Shapes** come from one atlas (`FxTextures`, 128-px cells, eight by eight since plan 25 · Y6: G8's 32 in the first four rows, the rest filled as the move batches ask): glows, sparks, rings and beams are light, added on top so they bloom; flames, drops, leaves, shards, rocks, feathers, petals, hearts, notes and letters are matter, alpha-blended and back to front. All are white or grey and take their type's colour. Y6 added six, all matter: a **fist** seen from the front (a rounded block, three knuckle lines, the thumb across), a **foot** from the side (a sole, a heel, toes to the right), an open **palm** (four fingers up, the thumb out), a narrowed **eye** (a lens of white, a dark iris and a glint), **cracks** running out from a point (a jagged line each way and two branches) and an **exclamation mark**. Y7 added five more, all matter: a **hexagon** of a shield (a pale panel, a brighter rim and a gleam across its upper half), a **target** (a ring with four ticks pointing in and a dot in the middle), a **beak** from the side (pointing right, a line along its gape), a **horn** (a curved point, ridged at its base) and a **seed** (an oval with a seam down it).
- **A template per type and category** (`MoveFx`): a physical move lands with a flash of contact and a burst of its type (flames, drops, sparks, leaves, shards, rocks, feathers, bubbles, wisps, petals, stars); a special move sends something across (Fire a stream of flames, Water a stream of drops, Electric a bolt, Grass spinning leaves, Ice and Dragon and Steel a beam, Fighting and Normal an orb of power, Poison sludge in an arc, Ground an eruption under the target, Flying gusts, Psychic rings, Bug a swarm, Rock thrown rocks, Ghost a shadow orb, Dark rings, Fairy sparkles); a status move wraps the user in an aura of its colour, or sends waves at the foe. 146 moves have effects of their own (Thunder's bolt from the sky, Flamethrower, Surf's wave, Hyper Beam, Swords Dance, the fangs of Bite, claws and slashes, Earthquake's shockwaves, sound waves for Growl, hearts for Charm, powders, weather).
- **Moves of their own** (plan 25 · Y6 to Y10, then Y11 to Y15 for the later games' moves): every move is given a look and a sound of its own, a batch at a time in the order the story shows them (`MoveSightings` in the tests: the first place, in plan 01's order of opening, where a trainer, a wild Pokémon at its level or a TM found or given shows the move; the water and the rods wait for HM03 and each rod). **Beats read, shapes and sounds our own**: each batch first reads the original's animation script of each move for its beats only (its parts in order, when it lands, whether the background darkens, who shakes, the names of its sounds); its particle files, sprites and palettes are art and are never opened. A recipe lives in the batch's file (`MoveFx.Platinum1.cs` for Y6's first chapter, and on), built from the kit's primitives, and replaces G8's look of the move where there was one. **What every recipe holds to**: it lands at 0.35 s with the damage (`FxList.Land`, which the landing sets, and which a recipe that lands its own way sets itself; a status move "lands" when it reaches its target or wraps its user), it is over by 1.3 s, it flashes at most 0.5 and under half a second, and at no age does it draw more quads and ribbons than `MoveFx.MaxQuads` (124: the heaviest of G8's looks, Surf's wave on a critical, super-effective hit, as Y6 measured it; a batch's look that wants more is cut down, the cap is never raised). **Its sound** is `use_<move>` (`docs/sound-effects.md`, "Moves"); a move whose batch hasn't come plays its type's template and its type's sound. The moves that are the user's own voice (Growl, Roar, Hyper Voice, Howl, Chatter) are heard as its cry in modes of their own. **The ground**: a cue carries the battle's terrain, and Secret Power's look goes by it (leaves on grass, sand and the marsh's mud as grains, a spray on water, rocks in a cave or on a mountain, ice on snow, rings in the League's and the Frontier's halls, a blow of stars anywhere else), as Camouflage's wash takes the ground's colours. Y6's first chapter, in its own words: Leer is a red eye narrowing over the user twice, glinting, and red rings rolling to the foe; Pound is an open hand swinging in and slapping; Scratch three white claw lines raked down, Fury Swipes two sets crossing; Tackle dust kicked up as the user runs and a ring where it meets; Withdraw rings of shell closing in round the user; Bide a red glow beating round it; Return gold stars round the user flying to the target; Quick Attack a white streak left behind; Fake Out two hands clapping before the target with a small flash; Poison Gas purple clouds that swell round the target and linger; Screech pale pink waves and the air round the target jittering; Growth leaves spiralling up the user; Leech Life a sting and red motes drawn back; Low Kick a foot sweeping in low with dust; Teleport a column of pink light rising through the user; Water Sport and Mud Sport drops thrown up round the user and raining back; Defense Curl a pale swirl curling in; Rock Smash a fist, cracks and stone thrown off; Aerial Ace a long bright slash after the speed lines; Astonish a wisp leaping and an exclamation mark over the target's head; Brick Break the edge of a hand chopping down and the thing cracking; Counter the user flaring orange and a fist back; Flash a white burst flooding the screen (0.45); Focus Punch a fist gathered before the user and flying in heavy; Force Palm a palm and pale blue rings of force with sparks; Rock Polish grit swirled off and the user glinting; Take Down dust, a hard blow and stars over the user from the recoil; Harden a grey sheen climbing it; Headbutt a ring and stars circling the target's head; Pursuit a dark shadow streaking after it; Rock Throw two stones lobbed and dust where they land; Stealth Rock five pointed stones lobbed to settle round the foe's platform (what lies there after is "What lies on the field"); Focus Energy orange streaks rushing into the user and its aura rising. Growl (its notes in a warm tint), Supersonic (pale yellow rings), Tail Whip (two white arcs of the swish), Absorb, Bubble, Ember, Splash, Water Gun, Earthquake and Rock Slide keep G8's looks, now theirs with a sound of their own. Y7's first part (Route 204 to Team Galactic's Eterna Building, sixty-seven moves): String Shot white threads shot across and wound round the target in bands; Rock Tomb four rocks dropped round the target one after another, each with dust and a jolt, standing a moment; Water Pulse a ball of water rolling across in pulsing rings and breaking in rings; Bullet Seed a rapid string of seeds, a pop where each lands; Charge sparks drawn into the user and little bolts crackling round it as its glow swells; Charm hearts hopping up round the user and one big heart floating to the foe; Leech Seed three seeds lobbed onto the target, sprouting leaves; Poison Sting a long needle and a purple puff; Stun Spore a yellow cloud puffed over the target that sprinkles down crackling; Wing Attack two wings sweeping in from either side and crossing, feathers flying; Pluck a beak jabbing three times; Peck a beak jabbing twice; Baton Pass a baton spun up over the user, glinting; Foresight a target ring closing on the foe and a glint in it; Lock-On the same ring tightening in steps, red; Karate Chop the edge of a hand chopping across; Mega Drain and Giga Drain green motes spiralling back to the user, which glows pale (Giga Drain more of them, with leaves); Mud Bomb a ball of mud lobbed and bursting in splatter; Mud-Slap clumps of mud flung from the user's feet, splattering; Rage the user flaring red, an anger mark, and the blow; Sand Attack two handfuls of sand fanned at the target and a cloud left over it; Sonic Boom three pale crescents in quick succession; Hypnosis the user's eyes glowing and rings rolling to the target, a swirl over its head; Thunderbolt three bolts struck down onto the target from above with sparks and a pale flash (0.25); Bite fangs closing once; Crunch fangs closing hard and shards breaking off; Feint Attack dark smoke where the user was and dark smoke at the target's far side as the blow lands; Toxic purple sludge welling up round the target and popping; Agility zigzag streaks either side of the user, twice; Aqua Jet a jet of water from the user to the target and its spray; Body Slam a wide ring on the ground where the weight lands, and dust; Confuse Ray a wobbling orb of pale light circling the target's head; Fire Punch a flaming fist and flames; Fire Spin a column of flame spiralling round the target; Hidden Power six orbs circling the user, then converging on the target; Magnet Bomb four steel orbs arcing in and bursting in sparks; Rain Dance dark clouds gathering high over the field and rain falling from them; Spark the user crackling yellow, sparks on the target; Swift a fan of stars curving in with trails; Thunder Wave a ring of electricity tightening round the target, crackling; Confusion pink rings rippling on the target, wobbling; Gust a whirlwind at the target, feathers in it; Horn Attack a horn thrust twice; Rollout a rolling wheel of dust carried in; Sleep Talk Z's rising and a pale wave of a mumble; Curse the user flaring red and dark wisps rising round it; Endure a red pulse bracing round the user; Frustration an anger mark popping and a flurry of blows; Mean Look a great dark eye opening over the target and purple rings closing in; Meditate calm rings and motes floating up round the user; Protect a pale hexagonal shield before the user, glinting; Reflect a wall of hexagonal panels before the user and a glint sweeping across; Safeguard a soft ring of light settling round the user, sparkles in it; Spite a pale wisp drawn out of the target, flickering; Night Shade a dark aura round the user and a ribbon of shadow to the target; Substitute a puff of smoke and a pop; Cut one long slash; Thief a hand darting in and back with a glint; Grass Knot blades of grass rising and tightening round the target's feet; Magical Leaf leaves spiralling in with sparkling trails; Razor Leaf spinning blades in a flat arc; Smokescreen a ball of smoke lobbed that bursts into a dark cloud; Sunny Day a sun over the field with rays and a warm glow (0.3); Recycle three arrows circling the user; Taunt a hand beckoning over the user and an anger mark over the target; Night Slash a dark crescent slashed across, then a second. Y7's second part (Route 206 to Route 208, thirty-nine moves, `MoveFx.Platinum3.cs`, batch `Y7b`, no new shape in the atlas): Double Team four pale afterimages sliding out either side of the user, swaying, and home again; Endeavor the user flaring yellow four times as four small blows land; Flame Wheel a wheel of flame winding up round the user, rolling across and breaking into fire on the target; Fury Cutter three cuts one after another, each longer and brighter, green scraps flying; Knock Off a fist driven up under the target's head and the thing it held popping off, spinning away; Magnitude the ground heaving in three jolts, cracking under the target and throwing up rocks, how hard being the seed's (the shake runs from 0.45 to 1); Roar (a look only; the sound is the user's cry) great arcs of voice rolling over the target and wind streaking past it and away; Seismic Toss streaks flinging the target up and bringing it down, the ground answering twice; Self-Destruct the user glowing hotter and swelling, sparks drawn in, then going off (flash 0.45); Explosion the same on a greater scale, a fireball and a pillar of smoke standing over the field; Slam a whipping tail ribbon coming over and down, a ring on the ground; Stomp a great foot dropped on the target from above, a shadow growing under it, held a moment; Block three slabs closing in left, right and over the target, locking with a clang; Imprison five bars dropped in turn in front of the target, a seal ringing the ground; Detect a bright eye opening over the user's head with rays and a halo pulsing twice; Mind Reader an eye over the target's head and a band of light scanning down its body twice; Disable the colour draining out of the target, a dark cross stamped on it and grey smoke sinking; Dragon Rage three ribbons of blue-violet fire weaving across and the blast they break into, shaking twice; Extrasensory three pulses of force sent over, each squeezing rings in on the target; Sand Tomb sand whirling up round the target in a narrowing spiral; Sandstorm gusts of sand streaking across the whole field and a haze drifting through; Tickle feathers brushing over the target in sways, sparkles and giggling waves; Uproar rings of noise spreading from the user again and again, notes bursting from it; Double Slap an open hand from the left, then one from the right; Follow Me a shaft of light on the user and stars popping over it three times; Sing three wavering lines of a stave from the user to the target with notes riding them, and Z's gathering on it; Bug Bite a pair of jaws snapping across the target twice, green juice flying; Encore five beats of a clap, each a ring and a flick of notes and stars, arrows circling the target; Flail the user thrashing in streaks of agitation, then a flurry of little blows; Ice Fang two great fangs of ice closing, frost and shards thrown off; Lucky Chant a golden ring turning on the ground round the user's side with stars riding it and a column of light; Mimic a pale ghost of the target lifting off it and floating across to the user; Rest a glow settling, a bubble swelling at the nose, Z's and little green crosses of healing; Revenge a dark red aura gathering, then a fist driven in trailing red; Rock Blast a volley of four rocks flung one after another, chips where each lands; Snore a big Z and great arcs of snore sent over, rumbling; Sweet Scent pink petals drifting across in lazy curves, a haze and sparkles on the target; Twister a funnel of wind standing up on the target, debris whirling in it; Worry Seed a seed lobbed high onto the target's head that splits, sweat flying and a small grey cloud gathering over it. Y8's first part (Hearthome City's gate to the Café Cabin on Route 210, fifty-one moves, `MoveFx.Platinum4.cs`, batch `Y8`, no new shape in the atlas; Y8's second part is `Y8b`): Bubble Beam a stream of bubbles with a body behind each to the target, which bob and sway round it before they pop; Mach Punch speed lines and a fist with three ghosts trailing it; Metal Claw the user glinting steel and two silver claws raked across the target, crossing; Future Sight an eye opening over the user and rings of foresight rolling to the foe, a haze turning over it; Pain Split red-pink sparks flowing each way, crossing, both glowing in turn and shuddering; Psybeam a ribbon of three wobbling colours with rings running down it and the target tinted purple; Shadow Ball shadow gathering into a dark orb that is thrown and bursts in a cloud; Shadow Claw a cloud of shadow thickening behind the target and two great ghostly claws raked across it; Shadow Sneak a dark streak along the ground and a shadowy hand striking up from under the target; Spit Up a great glob spat out and bursting in drops; Stockpile energy drawn in twice, a star kept over the head for each; Sucker Punch a dark fist from the target's blind side; Swallow the stored energy drawn into the mouth, a gulp and a healing light; Will-O-Wisp pale blue wisps circling the user, streaming across and setting the target alight; Minimize rings and arrows closing in on the user; Payback a dark gold aura, four heavy blows and coins flying; Attract a ring of pink hearts circling the target's head, more floating up; Amnesia thoughts drifting up out of the user's head as pale wisps; Aqua Ring a veil of water settling as a turning ring, bubbles riding it; Copycat twin trails spiralling from the foe to the user, which flashes gold; Fury Attack needles jabbing in from either side in quick turns; Jump Kick a foot in a high arc and down on the target, spinning, with ghosts of it behind; Light Screen a curtain of golden light rising before the user, shimmering, a glint running along it; Nasty Plot two red eyes narrowing over the user and dark wisps coiling up; Refresh a soft shower of light falling and washing through; Soft-Boiled a cream egg forming over the user, cracking, healing light spilling; Steel Wing two blades of steel crossing over the target with glints; Sweet Kiss a big heart popping over the head, small ones bursting from it and stars circling in a daze; Whirlwind a great wind swirling across the field and carrying the target with it, leaves and dust flying; Chatter (a look only; the sound is the user's cry) notes of every colour bursting from the mouth in a babble; Mirror Move (nothing of its own in the original) a mirror of silver light before the user, flashing; Haze a grey mist rolling across the whole field; Defog a bright wind sweeping the field, shoving grey cloud before it; Dig dirt spurting at the user's feet, a mound running along the ground and the user bursting up under the target; Yawn a drowsy cloud breathed out and drifting to the target, Z's rising; Acupressure three points of the body pressed in turn, each pinging a ring; Air Cutter two crescents of wind crossing on the target; False Swipe one clean pale slash pulled at the last, ending in a soft ring and stars; Feint two false thrusts flickering in before the true one; Metronome a pendulum swinging beside the user, ticking, and a ring of every colour popping where it settles; Pin Missile three needles lobbed one after another, each sinking in with a flash; Roost white feathers floating down onto the user and a healing light; Scary Face a hideous face snapping up over the target, two red eyes and a row of fangs, flickering; Shock Wave sparks gathering and a crooked bolt jumping straight across, crackling over the target; Slash three quick bright cuts, the last the longest, a flare at the end of each; Swagger the user puffing up in a ring of stars, the target stung red with an anger mark popping twice; Thunder Shock a small hard crack of electricity on the target, forks of it leaping off; Toxic Spikes five black-purple spikes lobbed to the foe's side and standing in the ground, a poison puff at each; Wake-Up Slap four quick slaps from alternate sides, the last waking it with a bang and Z's flying off; Cosmic Power a night sky closing round the user, stars drifting up through it and three small worlds in orbit; Lick a long tongue sweeping up across the target, leaving it slimed. Y8's second part (Route 210's northern half to the Seven Stars Restaurant, fifty-five moves, `MoveFx.Platinum5.cs`, batch `Y8b`, no new shape in the atlas): Air Slash a great pale crescent of air and a thinner one behind it, crossing the target with a white flare; Ancient Power rocks rising round the user in a gold-brown glow, flung in a ring and bursting on the target in stones and dust; Aqua Tail the user wound up in a coil of water, then a whip of water cracking down over the target with drops and a ring on the ground; Submission two hands gripping the target, a turning ring of speed arcs and five blows; Vital Throw a hammer-throw circle of arcs about the target and a heavy slam, the ground ringing; Whirlpool a column of water turning about the target on a swirl of the ground, drops circling; Bone Rush a cream bone spun in from the left and a second after it, dust where they land; Captivate a wink-star glinting over the user, a stream of small hearts and sparkles floating to the foe; Drain Punch a fist driven in, then green motes drawn back to the user, which glows; Fly (its charge half is the type's template and the user is not hidden: those are the body's) a spiral of wind at the user's feet and a white streak falling from the sky onto the target, feathers behind it; Recover gold rings of light pulsing up the user and healing crosses rising; Snatch a dark hand reaching to the target, taking a gold orb and carrying it back to the user; Strength the user flaring orange and two great palms shoving in, a ring and dust; Ice Beam a pale cyan beam carrying snowflakes and ice growing up round the target; Poison Fang purple fangs closing and poison dripping from them; Poison Jab two purple fists jabbing in quick turns, droplets flying; Signal Beam a beam changing red, green and yellow, the target flashing red and green; Sludge Bomb a great ball of sludge lobbed over that bursts in splatter and runs down the target; Tri Attack a red, a yellow and a blue orb gathered round the user and flown in one after another, each bursting in its own colour; Gyro Ball a steel ball spun up in two tilted rings and driven in; X-Scissor two long green blades crossing in an X on the target; Horn Drill a horn boring in three times, spirals round it; Last Resort four coloured orbs of the user's moves drawn in and two heavy blows, the second the greater; Y9a (MoveFx.Platinum6.cs, batch `Y9`, Lake Valor to Route 222, 54 moves, no new shape in the atlas; **anything high over the target is off the picture**: the overview shows about one body's height above a Pokémon, so a falling thing starts within 1.3 *h* of it and a lobbed one arcs under 0.1 of its way): Psych Up pink motes drawn out of the foe and swirled to the user, which glows and rings; Thunder a dark cloud over the foe, a small bolt and then a great one with a flash, sparks jumping; Aurora Beam three ribbons of red, green and blue waving across and ringing the target; Barrier five hexagonal panes standing up in an arc by the user, flashing along their edges; Close Combat six fists and feet from every side, each with a spark; Dizzy Punch two punches and the target ringed by stars and a swirl; High Jump Kick dust, a streak climbing and a foot coming down; Cross Chop two hands brought down in turn and two bars crossing in a star; Ice Shard snow drawn into a long shard that glints and is flung across; Swords Dance four blades circling the user turning and drawn down in a flash; Wood Hammer a great log raised and brought down, leaves flying, a crack in the ground and a jolt back on the user; Heal Bell rings and gold and cyan notes spreading from the user, a pale wash; Psywave three wobbling violet and pink bands crossing and breaking in rings; Fire Blast a flame ball gathered and flown across, bursting into a five-pointed star of fire with a flash; Flamethrower a steady thick stream of flame that goes on burning on the target; Punishment a dark whip lashing twice and red exclamation marks popping up one more each time; Conversion 2 a scanning grid sweeping over the foe, then rings on the user changing colour one after another; Icy Wind snowflakes blown across and frost settling on the foe, tinted pale blue; Shadow Punch a dark violet fist with three ghosts of itself behind it and a cold ring; Hail white stones slanting down across both sides and bouncing; Rock Climb stone steps heaving up between the two and a charge up them; Avalanche slabs of snow and ice sliding in a white cloud onto the target; Blizzard a white storm of snowflakes whirling across, a cloud settling on the foe and crystals growing round it; Focus Blast orange rings drawing in, a great orb thrown and a ring of fire-coloured light bursting; Discharge bolts leaping from the user every way, one reaching the foe; Dragon Dance a blue serpent of light coiling up the user and three red-gold rings beating; Hydro Pump a wide braided jet of water, a great splash and a ring; Muddy Water a heaving wall of brown water breaking over the target; Thunder Fang yellow fangs snapping shut with a bolt between them; Zen Headbutt rings of thought closing on the brow, an orb of will rammed in and rings breaking out. Y9b (`MoveFx.Platinum7.cs`, the Sunyshore Gym to Route 224, in the same words): Super Fang two great white fangs closing and a second clack, stars off the bite; Mirror Shot glints on the user's body and a ball of mirror-light bursting in a silver flash; Charge Beam yellow sparks gathering and crackling, then a thick yellow beam held on the target, which glows with it; Giga Impact a cloud of white drawn in, a long shower of speed and a great star, rings and a ring on the ground; Octazooka three balls of black ink spat across, bursting in ink, smoke and drips; Wring Out four twisting bands wringing the target three times, drops flying; Dark Pulse black and violet rings sent out one after another and breaking over the target, stained dark; Dragon Pulse a braid of teal and violet light, three rings of force and a great ring; Dragon Rush a coiling blue serpent of light with a snapping jaw and a ring where it meets; Hammer Arm a great fist raised and brought down, the ground cracking, stones thrown up; Hyper Beam a hot ball gathered at the mouth and a huge orange beam held, shaking the target, ending in a great burst; Natural Gift the berry in the colours of its type drawn out and thrown, spraying its juice and leaves; Power Whip two thick green vines lashing in turn and crossing; Brave Bird the user in blue-white flame with a great beak at its head, feathers flying and a red ring of recoil on the user; Head Smash a head driven in with a crack of stone, rocks, a split in the ground and a red ring on the user; Leaf Storm leaves whirling up round the user and a storm of them flung across, blowing round the target; Attack Order a swarm of little bugs pouring across in lines and stinging the target all over; Bug Buzz green and yellow rings of sound with notes, the target shuddering; Cross Poison two violet slashes crossing in an X with venom dripping; Defend Order a swarm circling the user, golden plates flashing up and a red-gold glow; Heal Order the same swarm with green motes rising, a white glow and crosses; Iron Head the head gleaming steel, rammed in twice with a ring of metal; Megahorn a huge horn thrust in, rays flying from the puncture; Power Gem a pink-white gem of light spinning and shot across, scattering shards; Earth Power three glowing columns of earth bursting up under the target in turn; Rock Wrecker stones packed into a boulder, flung and shattering; Flare Blitz the user wrapped in flame and hurtling in as a comet, a burst on the target and a red ring of recoil; Overheat the user glowing red and trembling, then a vast burst of white-yellow flame and a ring of fire on the ground; Solar Beam sunlight drawn in a bright bloom and a thick golden-green beam held on the target; Energy Ball green light swelling into a ball that is thrown and blooms on the target with leaves; Leaf Blade two green blades crossing in an X, leaves off the cuts; Aura Sphere blue rings drawn into a sphere that is fired with a ring turning round it; Extreme Speed a streak of white, ghosts of the user and three quick blows; Mirror Coat a pane of silver glass flashing and a beam of pink light sent back; Surf a great wave rolling the length of the field and crashing over the target in foam; Heat Wave waves of scorching air, orange and red, with embers, the foe tinted hot; Acid two blobs of acid, purple and green, splashing and fizzing; Constrict three pink feelers coiling and squeezing in small knocks; Razor Wind a whirl gathered round the user and two crescents of keen wind let off in turn; Tailwind streaks of white air past the user's side, feathers and leaves with them, and a spiral of wind on the ground; Bullet Punch two steel fists in quick turn, each with a ring; Dive a splash and ring where the user goes under, ripples racing along the ground and a spout of water under the target; Dynamic Punch a fist wreathed in orange fire, a great star and two rings, the picture rocked; Guard Swap blue and cyan shield motes crossing between the two with a pane at each end; Gunk Shot a heavy lump of filth hurled and splattering in a rank mess; Outrage the user wrapped in red with anger marks beating, dragon fire flung twice and the target ringed red; Perish Song grey and violet notes over both sides, a dark swirl under each Pokémon and a grey tint at the end; Power Swap the same crossing in red and orange fists; Sheer Cold a frost spreading under the target, spears of ice thrusting up round it, flakes falling and the target locked in white; Spore pale yellow-green spores drifting across and settling round the target; Acid Armor the user melting in wobbling green rings, drops plopping round it four times; Aromatherapy a lime mist with petals drifting down and healing crosses; Destiny Bond a purple eye over the user and a pulsing chain of dark light to the foe, ghost-fire on both; Gastro Acid a yellow-green cloud eating at the target's middle, ringed twice, dripping; Grudge black-violet flames round the user with red eyes opening and wisps drawn in; Mega Kick a giant foot kicking, rays off the blow and the picture rocked; Moonlight a pale moon rising behind the user, beams of moonlight, stars and crosses; Trick a puff of stars and the two items, a white ball and a gold one, whisked across each other's way and switched. Y10a (`MoveFx.Platinum8.cs`, the post-game from the Fuego Ironworks' sea to Stark Mountain, in the same words; Hyper Voice is the user's own cry and has only a look): Clamp two pink scalloped shells closing on the target and snapping tighter, drops and stars; Crabhammer a great teal fist coming down like a hammer, the ground cracking and a spray flung up; Double Kick two kicks from either side, a flash at each; Icicle Spear four blue icicles thrown across one after another and left standing, ice flying at each; Rapid Spin a white whirl winding up round the user and a tight one at the target, dust and sparks; Water Spout a jet thrown up from the user falling as heavy rain and a wave on the target; Belly Drum six rings of sound off the user's belly, a red glow and an anger mark; Covet hearts and stars about the user, a pat and a light whisked back to it; Slack Off the user slack in a soft white light, Z's and healing crosses; Superpower red-gold power drawn into the user and a vast fist driving in, the ground bursting; Blaze Kick a flaming foot swung in, trailing fire, bursting in flame; Eruption a fountain of fire thrown up from the user twice and a rain of burning rock and lava on the target, blast after blast; Facade the user flaring red, green and gold in turn and ramming in with a white star; Seed Bomb five seeds lobbed in a string, bursting one after another and twice more; Triple Kick three kicks, each larger than the one before; Mega Punch a huge pale fist, a white star and two rings of force; Skull Bash the user tucked in a spin, then a head-first charge and a star with a crack in the ground; Glare two huge slit eyes opening over the target and yellow sparks crawling over it; Healing Wish a column of white light and falling stars with hearts and crosses carrying the user's life away; Lava Plume molten drops thrown out in a fan from a plume at the user's feet; Sky Uppercut streaks of sky racing upward and a fist rising from below, a white beam and stars; Cotton Spore soft cotton puffs floating across and smothering the target in a pale cloud; Crush Claw two heavy slashes of pale claws and a crack across the target; Fissure cracks racing out and a black chasm opening under the target, rock and dust thrown up; Needle Arm a thorny green fist jabbing four times, needles flying off each; Spikes iron caltrops thrown in arcs and left lying on the ground by the foes, glinting; Teeter Dance the user swaying in a ring of swirls and notes and giddy stars over the foe; Guillotine two steel pincers closing from either side and a white blade of light through the target; Present a wrapped box lobbed across, opening in confetti and stars; Heal Block a ring of crimson seals round the target and a heart struck through with a dark cross, a grey pall; Hyper Voice two bellows, three arcs of sound each, the target shaking; Psycho Shift the user flaring white and a spiral of violet motes drawn across to the target, which flares violet. Y10b (`MoveFx.Platinum9.cs` and `Platinum10.cs`, the 64 moves the story shows nowhere, in the same words; Howl is the user's own cry and has only a look): Comet Punch four small fists, each trailing a star; Pay Day gold coins flung across and raining on the target; Vise Grip two jaws of steel teeth closing from either side and squeezing; Rolling Kick the user spinning in, dust and speed, a foot round in a wide arc; Twineedle two thin needles thrown twice and left standing, the target flushed with poison; Sleep Powder pale blue-green powder sifting down, Z's rising; Sludge a ball of purple filth splattering, drips running; Bone Club a long bone swung round and brought down, dust and stars; Spike Cannon a rapid volley of twelve spikes and a clatter of them standing in the target; Barrage round balls lobbed one after another, each bursting in a ring and a puff; Lovely Kiss a great pink kiss blown across, hearts bursting off it, Z's; Sky Attack a golden wrap of light, a long white-gold flare of feathers and a great burst; Transform the user breaking into grey-blue tiles that flicker and swirl and re-form; Bonemerang a bone thrown out and spinning back, striking twice; Sharpen a ring of white blades spinning out and glints over the outline; Conversion hexagons of four colours spun up one after another and melted into one glow; Struggle a weak flailing rush and a red ring of recoil on the user; Sketch a pencil line drawn round the target in loops, the copy flashing; Spider Web white threads shot across and spread as spokes and rings, binding;
