Nightmare a dark violet swirl and two red eyes, Z's; Aeroblast three twisting ribbons of cyan air and rings of wind bursting; Powder Snow a flurry of fine snow settling and frosting the foes; Zap Cannon a great yellow orb charged and fired in a trail of lightning, a ring of bolts; Milk Drink white swirling milk, drops and bubbles, hearts; Sacred Fire a rising spiral of gold-red flame and a great sacred flame with a ring of light; Morning Sun a gold sun climbing behind the user, spokes of light and crosses; Flatter a shower of petals and stars and a pink swirl of praise over the foe; Smelling Salts a little vial under the nose, green-white fumes and a jolt; Nature Power the ground's own things (leaves, drops, stones, snow, sand, sparks) spiralling up and thrown across; Role Play a pale double of the foe stepping over the user; Magic Coat a bubble of pink-white hexagonal panels with a sheen sweeping over it; Arm Thrust open palms thrust again and again from alternate sides; Tail Glow the tail's tip flaring into a dazzling star with long rays; Luster Purge a ball of silver-white light and glitter bursting in crystal stars; Mist Ball a ball of pink and blue mist bursting in a great puff and fine droplets; Feather Dance grey feathers whirling down in a slow spiral; Ice Ball a ball of packed ice rolling across, breaking in shards and a chill; Blast Burn flames wheeling round the user and a vast ball of fire going off in a great blast; Hydro Cannon a great ball of water fired with a roaring jet behind it, a huge splash; Weather Ball a swirl of the weather's colour gathered, flashed and thrown, bursting in it; Fake Tears big blue tears streaming down, a few landing on the foe; Howl rings of red sound rolling up and out, anger and a red glow; Frenzy Plant thorned vines bursting from the ground and lashing; Bulk Up red-orange pulses, two fists pumping and anger marks; Poison Tail a tail of violet light swung in a crescent, poison dripping; Volt Tackle the user cloaked in lightning, a long trail and a ring of bolts, a ring of recoil on the user; Doom Desire a golden star drawn together over the user and sent rising; Psycho Boost a violet-white charge, rings drawn in and a great wave of force over the target; Trump Card four playing cards flicked across, the last with a great star; Power Trick a red orb and a blue one circling the user and swapping twice; Me First light drawn out of the foe to the user and a bolt of it thrown back; Heart Swap pink hearts streaming both ways and crossing; Vacuum Wave three small whirling crescents of air and a punch of wind; Switcheroo three cups shuffled, lifted on the items under them, which are whisked across; Draco Meteor a rain of burning blue-violet meteors, each ending in a blast, the last a vast one; Judgment five pillars of white-gold light and a halo, a great white flash; Roar of Time a clock face of rings and turning hands, a great beam and a second clock turning over the target; Spacial Rend a pearl-white slash and a dark rift full of stars; Lunar Dance a crescent moon and a ring of pale stars dancing; Crush Grip two vast open palms closing and squeezing, red rings tightening; Magma Storm a spiral of fire, lava and burning stones winding up in a column; Dark Void a black hole and a violet-black swirl over the foes, Z's drifting out; Seed Flare a point of white-green light grown into a dazzling flare flung across, a bloom of light and leaves; Shadow Force the user melting into a violet-black shadow that slides across and rises in dark claws. **Y11a, the later games' moves, first part** (`MoveFx.Later1.cs` and `Later1b.cs`, batch `Y11`; plan 25 · Y11): Generation 5's Hone Claws to Bulldoze (numbers 468 to 523). No game's animation is the model (the decompilation holds none of these): each look comes from the move's data (type, category, contact, target, effect, description) and this vocabulary, our own shapes and sounds. A move cast on the user's side but meant for the foes, or on the whole field, puts its look between the two (`c.Foe`). Hone Claws three dark claws rising beside the user with a glint whetted down each edge; Wide Guard a broad row of tan hexagonal panels across the user's side with rocks at its foot; Guard Split two blue shields (and Power Split two red fists) sliding together, meeting in a flash and parting in each other's colour; Wonder Room a violet-teal ring on the ground between the two with two arrows trading places; Psyshock pink orbs breaking into crystal shards; Venoshock a heavy pour of violet venom, bubbling; Autotomize flakes of grey metal peeling off and arrows rising; Rage Powder a cloud of red-orange spore dust, whirling spores and an anger mark; Telekinesis pink rings lifting the foe, motes drifting up and a thread of force; Magic Room a magenta ring with round items floating up in it, each crossed out and winking away; Smack Down a great rock dropped on the target, the ground cracking; Storm Throw an orange whirl spinning the target and a fist slamming it down; Flame Burst a fireball bursting and flames flying out sideways; Sludge Wave a thick purple wall of sludge; Quiver Dance petals in green, rose and sky blue circling the user in rings; Heavy Slam a vast steel slab flattening the target, the ground ringing; Synchronoise rings pulsing out of both in time with a wavering thread between; Electro Ball a crackling yellow ball with leaping bolts; Soak a drenching downpour and a puddle ringing out; Flame Charge the user cloaked in fire with a trail of flames left on its path; Coil a violet ribbon winding up the user in a tightening spiral; Low Sweep a leg whipped round low across the feet, dust kicked up; Acid Spray acid-green globs spat across, sizzling and dripping; Foul Play a shadow double of the foe stepping out and a dark fist swung back through it; Simple Beam a thin pale beam and a bare ring with an equals sign over the foe's head; Entrainment a line of notes flowing across and rings beating on each; After You an open hand beckoning, chevrons pointing the way; Round two rings of notes circling the foe in opposite ways; Echoed Voice a ring of sound followed by paler, smaller echoes; Chip Away three quick chisel strokes and flying chips; Clear Smog a thick purple smog cloud swept clear by pale streaks; Stored Power pink stars saved up circling the user, then all let fly; Quick Guard one tall orange screen snapping up with streaks glancing off; Ally Switch two glowing silhouettes trading places in pink swirls; Scald a jet of near-boiling water with rolling steam and a red heat; Shell Smash the shell bursting into cream-white pieces on a ring, red sparks out and blue in; Heal Pulse soft pink pulses rolling to the target and ringing it twice, hearts rising; Hex a purple hexagram of six hexes turning and tightening, wisps rising; Sky Drop a whirl of wind and feathers lifting the target, two talons closing, a drop; Shift Gear two interlocking cogs turning against each other, sparks and speed lines; Circle Throw a ring of orange speed swinging round the foe and a fist throwing it; Incinerate flames over the target and a red berry burnt to grey ash; Quash a vast dark hand pressing the foe flat; Acrobatics a looping ribbon of feathers and two quick cuts; Reflect Type a pale mirror panel and the foe's colours pouring across to the user; Retaliate pale spirits of fallen friends rising and rushing in with the user; Final Gambit a blinding gold-white gathering fired in one beam, the user's ring fading; Bestow a small gift tied with a pink ribbon lobbed across in hearts; Inferno a spiral of red and violet-tinged flame in a roaring pillar; Water Pledge a tall pale-blue column of water winding with bubbles; Fire Pledge a column of fire with raining embers and a burning ring; Grass Pledge thick green vines bursting up round the target; Volt Switch a bolt and a streak back in a U-turn with an arrow turning over; Struggle Bug a scuttling mob of little green bugs swarming the target; Bulldoze a line of rocks and a heaving ridge of earth ploughed to the target. **Y11b** (`MoveFx.Later2.cs`, batch `Y11b`, plan 25 · Y11b: the other 36 of Generation 5, Frost Breath to Fusion Bolt, in the same way) and **Y12** (`MoveFx.Later3.cs` and `Later3b.cs`, batch `Y12`: Generation 6's 62 moves, Flying Press to Hyperspace Fury): each look from the move's data in our own shapes, none of them a new atlas cell. Y11b: Frost Breath a wide breath of snow with a star at the landing; Dragon Tail a thick indigo lash and arrows streaking the foe away; Work Up red rings and up arrows climbing either side; Electroweb a yellow net opening and tightening over the foe; Wild Charge the user cloaked in lightning with sparks leaping back; Drill Run a turning grey cone grinding in; Dual Chop two violet blades crossing in an X; Heart Stamp a pink heart stamped down and left as a print; Horn Leech a white horn and green life drawn back to the user; Sacred Sword a gold blade breaking a blue barrier; Razor Shell pearl-white scallop edges over water; Heat Crash the user dropping as a falling sun, the ground burning; Leaf Tornado a funnel of leaves, a brown haze after; Steamroller a spiked ball rolling over the foe; Cotton Guard white puffs wrapping the user; Night Daze rings of dark that blot out the foe's sight; Psystrike one long glassy lance; Tail Slap four quick swats; Hurricane a pale cyclone, feathers and stars; Head Charge a blunt white horn of force; Gear Grind two steel gears thrown in turn; Searing Shot a fan of red-hot fireballs raining round the foe; Techno Blast a cyan beam of square pixels and a grid of data blocks; Relic Song cream notes and a ring of turning runes; Secret Sword two gold crescents crossing; Glaciate a gust of cold and frost crystals standing at the foe's feet; Bolt Strike a huge blue-white bolt from the sky; Blue Flare deep blue fire round a white-hot core; Fiery Dance flames dancing round the user and thrown in fans; Freeze Shock a block of ice crackling with lightning; Ice Burn pale cyan flame and snow; Snarl dark wine-coloured waves from bared fangs; Icicle Crash three long icicles falling in turn; V-create two ribbons of flame closing in a V on the foe, grey arrows falling over the user; Fusion Flare a smoking orange fireball falling from the left and Fusion Bolt a crackling yellow sphere from the right. Y12: Flying Press a fist dropping between two wings of feathers; Mat Block three tan panels with a blue edge; Belch a green burp; Rototiller furrows and sprouts across the field; Sticky Web a silk web on the ground at the foe's feet; Fell Stinger two thin stabs; Phantom Force a violet rift over the foe and a spectral claw; Trick-or-Treat a pumpkin lantern and sweets circling; Noble Roar golden rings and stars; Ion Deluge a charged cloud over the field raining sparks; Parabolic Charge arcs of lightning over a high parabola; Forest's Curse dark teal vines with glowing eyes; Petal Blizzard a gale of pink petals across the field; Freeze-Dry a white-teal beam and dry vapour; Disarming Voice pink rings with hearts and notes; Parting Shot dark waves and the user streaking off; Topsy-Turvy two arrows turning the foe over; Draining Kiss a stream of hearts and a lip-mark; Crafty Shield a dome of pink-lilac hexagons; Flower Shield blossoms round both Pokémon; Grassy Terrain a meadow spreading over the field; Misty Terrain a lilac mist and sparkles; Electrify a coat of looping current; Play Rough a scuffle-cloud with fists and feet; Fairy Wind a slow stream of sparkles; Moonblast a crescent moon and a column of moonlight; Boomburst colossal white shock rings; Fairy Lock a gold padlock and a ring of chain; King's Shield a steel shield and a crown; Play Nice two hands clasping; Confide a speech bubble with three dots; Diamond Storm glittering diamonds; Steam Eruption a geyser of steam; Hyperspace Hole a violet ring and a hand out of it; Water Shuriken three spinning stars of water; Mystical Fire violet-pink flame; Spiky Shield a ring of thorns; Aromatic Mist lilac mist and petals; Eerie Impulse sickly green pulses; Venom Drench a flask smashed and a puddle; Powder falling yellow dust; Geomancy six pillars of light on gold rings; Magnetic Flux a red plus, a blue minus and arcs of force; Happy Hour a rain of coins; Electric Terrain a lattice of yellow-blue lines on the ground; Dazzling Gleam a blinding flare with long rays; Celebrate a party popper; Hold Hands a chain of hearts; Baby-Doll Eyes two huge shining eyes; Nuzzle red cheeks sparking; Hold Back a gentle pat held by a ring; Infestation a crowd of tiny dark bugs; Power-Up Punch a red fist and the user's arm flaring; Oblivion Wing red-black wings and a draining beam; Thousand Arrows a rain of earth arrows; Thousand Waves rolling waves of sand; Land's Wrath cracks and a column of earth; Light of Ruin a great pink flower of light and one wide beam; Origin Pulse three deep-blue orbs and crossing beams; Precipice Blades blades of red-hot rock; Dragon Ascent a green-gold spiral and a dive; Hyperspace Fury four rifts and a fist from each. **What the Y11b and Y12 looks taught** (the overview camera shows about 1.3 body heights above a Pokémon, and the player's own platform is close to the lens): a column, a gust or a confetti burst that rises 2 heights leaves the picture; additive rings and waves come out pale, so a dark or coloured ring is alpha-blended; a stock heal's aura (`HealOn`) lasts a second from its start, so a recipe that heals late draws its own, shorter one; a loop with no envelope draws on after the cue (the test finds it). **The darkened background** that the original's scripts ask for in many of these (Charge, Thunderbolt, Swift, Hidden Power, Curse, Mean Look, Spite, Night Shade, String Shot) is not drawn: a dim over the whole picture would darken the Pokémon and the effects with it, and the stage's own dim is a renderer's change of its own (plan 25, Y7's owed list).
- **Moves of their own, Y13a** (`MoveFx.Later4.cs`, `Later4b.cs` and `Later4c.cs`, batch `Y13`: Generation 7's 68 regular moves, Shore Up to Double Iron Bash, and Generation 8's first 13, Dynamax Cannon to Court Change; the Z-Moves and Max Moves between are Y14's and Y15's): each look from the move's data in our own shapes, none of them a new atlas cell. Shore Up a tan sand spiral and a green glow; First Impression a star flashing on the foe before a green-white double slash; Baneful Bunker a dome of purple hexagons with poison bubbles; Spirit Shackle an arrow pinning the foe's shadow in three violet rings; Darkest Lariat a black loop whipped twice round the foe; Sparkling Aria notes on a stream of bubbles; Ice Hammer a hammer of pale ice and frost; Floral Healing a pink blossom opening over the target; High Horsepower two hoof stamps and a ring of cracks; Strength Sap green sap drawn back to a healing user; Solar Blade a golden orb drawn into a blade of white light; Leafage a handful of leaves; Spotlight a cone of white light on a ring; Toxic Thread a violet thread winding round the foe; Laser Focus red rings closing on the user's eye and a taut line; Gear Up two steel gears and up arrows; Throat Chop a dark chop and sound waves crossed out; Pollen Puff a yellow puffball bursting in pollen; Anchor Shot an anchor dropped on a chain; Psychic Terrain pink ripples and rising lights on the ground; Lunge a green streak and mandibles; Fire Lash a whip of flame; Power Trip a dark red aura and a black fist; Burn Up flames swallowing the user and a wave across the field; Speed Swap streaks crossing; Smart Strike a red sight and a horn; Purify a drop of clear light and white rings; Revelation Dance petals and notes in the user's colour; Core Enforcer rings shrinking down a beam and a cross over the foe's head; Trop Kick a foot, leaves and blossom; Instruct arrows turning over the ally's head; Beak Blast a beak heating red; Clanging Scales ringing hexagons and sound rings; Dragon Hammer an indigo hammer and violet cracks; Brutal Swing a huge dark arc; Aurora Veil green, blue and pink curtains with snow; Shell Trap a ring of shell plates and a column of flame; Fleur Cannon a pink flower firing a beam; Psychic Fangs fangs and splintering panes; Stomping Tantrum an anger mark and two stamps; Shadow Bone a spinning bone in ghost-fire; Accelerock a grey streak and a rock; Liquidation a blade of water; Prismatic Laser a prism splitting one beam into five colours; Spectral Thief a ghost hand pulling arrows over; Sunsteel Strike a blazing steel comet; Moongeist Beam a dim moon and a violet beam; Tearful Look two huge wet eyes; Zing Zap a yellow bolt and a flinch; Nature's Madness whirling leaves and petals and a tightening ring; Multi-Attack six coloured orbs joined into one white ball; Mind Blown a head bursting in flame; Plasma Fists two crackling fists; Photon Geyser a column of violet light from the ground; Zippy Zap a zigzag of lightning and a star; Splishy Splash a wave with sparks; Floaty Fall feathers and a cloud dropped on the foe; Pika Papow a heart of lightning; Bouncy Bubble a bubble hopping three times; Buzzy Buzz a swarm of sparks in loops; Sizzly Slide a streak of flame along the ground; Glitzy Glow glitter and a pink pane; Baddy Bad a black cloud with a red grin and a red pane; Sappy Seed a seed and a vine that grows; Freezy Frost a swirl of snow and a ring; Sparkly Swirl a spiral of pink sparkles; Veevee Volley three paw swats and hearts; Double Iron Bash two steel fists; Dynamax Cannon three muzzle rings and a colossal crimson beam; Snipe Shot a red sight and one bead of water; Jaw Lock a pair of jaws and a chain between the two Pokémon; Stuff Cheeks berries popping into swelling cheeks; No Retreat a ring of red stakes; Tar Shot a black glob and dripping strings; Magic Powder purple glitter falling; Dragon Darts two darts in turn; Teatime a cup and saucer with steam in the middle of the field; Octolock six arms winding tight; Bolt Beak a crackling beak diving; Fishious Rend blue fangs and scales; Court Change two ground panels trading sides. **Y13a looks taught**: a pane, a vine, an anchor or a hand drawn at the foe's size is tiny at the overview's distance, so a prop standing in the picture is sized up (the anchor 1.5 times the foe) and a ribbon that is meant to be seen is 0.1 of the height or more; a thing placed at 'the user's cheek' or 'the user's mouth' goes by the body's middle (`c.From`), never by an offset from its feet; a beam's muzzle rings face the lens and are alpha-blended, or they come out pale.
- **Moves of their own, Y13b** (`MoveFx.Later5.cs`, `Later5b.cs` and `Later5c.cs`, batch `Y13b`: Generation 8's Clangorous Soul to Take Heart and Generation 9's Tera Blast to Malignant Chain, 145 in all): each look from the move's data in our own shapes, none a new atlas cell, and each different from its type's template and from the moves most like it (the Torque five by their exhaust, the three Storms by cold, cloud and sand, the two beams of recoil by their colour). A Tera move takes its type's colour from the cue. **Y13b looks taught**: tools, shields, apples and wheels are drawn 1.5 to 2 times the foe's size to read at the overview's distance; a beam is no wider than 0.38 of the user's height; a circle that should be seen whole stands in the picture's plane, not flat on the ground; the shake of a blow never exceeds 1.0; a look that adds a wave wall to its own waves goes over `MaxQuads`.
- **Signature moves** (plan 25 · Y17; `Graphics/Direction.cs`, GPU-free): the big attacks are *staged*, not only drawn. A `MoveDirection` of `Directions.Table` (by move name) is a short timeline over the move's own look: its camera shot ("Camera (G8)" above), its **hit-stop**, a body motion for its user and its target, a stage light, and an effect laid over its recipe, each a pure function of the cue's age and seed. Y17a built the table with six to prove it (Hyper Beam and Solar Beam push in, Roar of Time follows its wave, Close Combat and Fly punch in, Hurricane orbits); Y17b on fill it by whose the move is (the legendaries' and mythicals' own, the starters' finals' and the pseudo-legendaries', the advanced moves).
  - **Hit-stop is a shown age** (`EffectCue.ShownAge`, `Directions.Remap`): at a heavy impact the picture holds at 0.35 s for 80 ms (`Directions.HeavyStop`, times `BattleCamera.Motion`) and then catches up, faster, so the cue still ends at its own length. Everything drawn of the cue reads the shown age (its effect, its body motions, its stage light, its camera); the animator's clock, the line's impact and the bar's drain do not, so no line waits a frame longer. A miss, a blocked move, a charge turn, an attack sent ahead and the Z- and Max set pieces never stop (YD's Max Moves add theirs through the same remap).
  - **A heavy hit throws its target back** (`Directions.Thrown`): 0.12 of its height away from the user at the impact, settling over 0.5 s, with a squash of 0.08.
  - **Y17b's batch** (the legendaries' and mythicals' own of Sinnoh, Kanto, Johto and Hoenn, and the starters' finals'): a blow from above presses its target (flattened 0.14, sunk 0.06), a blow from under tosses it up (0.22 of its height and down in 0.6 s), a quake jolts it three times, a grip only shudders it; Dragon Ascent's user rises 0.3 over its wind-up and dives in to the impact; Eruption's and Overheat's user heaves down and stretches up as it bursts. **What a move leaves on the target's platform** is laid over after the impact and gone within 0.9 s: fire leaves embers, Ground and the meteors cracks (lying before the feet, never tilted up over the body), Grass leaves settling, Water spray falling back. A signature's wash over a move whose own stage already goes white or pale is kept under 0.2, or the impact washes out.
  - **Y17c's batch** (the pseudo-legendaries' moves and the starters' finals' of Unova to Paldea, Hisui's three among them): a charge that meets its target (Dragon Rush, Glaive Rush, Aqua Step) **lunges** the user 0.55 of the way in to the impact and back over 0.45 s, with no rise; a bite only shudders its target, a hammer or a body falling on it presses it, a blade of rock from under tosses it, a drum through the roots jolts it. What a move leaves is the Y17b set and five more, each on or over the target's platform, under ten quads and ribbons and gone within 0.9 s: **shards** of rock falling round the feet (Stone Edge, Head Smash, Ceaseless Edge's splinters), **stars** springing off the body in gold and white (Meteor Mash), **rings of sound** standing round the target and widening, gold (Clanging Scales, Clangorous Soul on its user), **frost** glinting on the platform in snowflakes (Glaive Rush, an ice dragon's), and **petals** drifting down (Flower Trick). A projectile of the later starters' (Pyro Ball, Snipe Shot, Spirit Shackle, Water Shuriken, Mystical Fire, Torch Song's song) is followed as Y17a's are; a combo of rage or song round the target (Outrage, Sparkling Aria, Clanging Scales) orbits. Dark Pulse's wash is the dark vignette of the plan: a near black at 0.4 over the move's own grey. Outrage has no wash of its own: its own red fade already takes the whole stage.
  - **Y17d's batch** (the legendaries' and mythicals' own of Unova, Kalos, Alola, Galar, Hisui and Paldea): a legend's charge (V-create, Bolt Strike, Sunsteel Strike, Behemoth Blade and Bash, Collision Course, Electro Drift) lunges as Y17c's do; a lance or arrows from above press the target (Thousand Arrows, Glacial Lance, Double Iron Bash, Ivy Cudgel), waves of earth jolt it, a chain only shudders it; a beam wound up (Blue Flare, Photon Geyser, Prismatic Laser, Fleur Cannon, Eternabeam, Dragon Energy) rocks its user back; a storm, song or cage round the foes orbits; a move on its own side (Geomancy, Jungle Healing) has no shot. Two reactions join the set, each under ten quads and gone within 0.8 s of the impact: **sparks** crackling round the body in pale yellow, each jumping to a new place every tenth of a second (the electric legends, Freeze Shock, Techno Blast), and **glints** of crystal or light standing round the body and turning, pale pink, blue and gold (Diamond Storm, Necrozma's two, Core Enforcer, Sunsteel Strike, Geomancy, Tachyon Cutter). A wash over a move whose own look already flashes white or goes pale (Prismatic Laser, Tera Starstorm, Eternabeam, Dynamax Cannon, Bolt Strike) is kept at 0.1 to 0.24.
  - **Y17e's batch** (the advanced and special moves, with the Pledges, the protections, Blood Moon, Matcha Gotcha, Hisui's non-legends' own and the two legends' moves left before): a beam or a blast wound up (Sky Attack, Focus Blast, Blood Moon, Chloroblast) is pushed in on and rocks its user back; a ball or a cannon is followed (Shadow Ball, Flash Cannon, Barb Barrage, Bitter Malice, Esper Wing); a body flung in lunges (Brave Bird, Headlong Rush, Wave Crash, Psyshield Bash); the two-turn moves' strikes are punched in on, Dig's and Dive's tossing their target up from under it and Bounce's user rising and diving as Dragon Ascent's does; a wave, a storm of snow or heat, a whirlpool or a rampage round the foes orbits (Surf, Blizzard, Heat Wave, Whirlpool, Thrash, Matcha Gotcha, Raging Fury); a Pledge's pillar is punched in on, without a stop, and tosses its target. A move on its own side has no shot: a **setup** dance or thought (Swords Dance, Nasty Plot, Calm Mind, Dragon Dance, Victory Dance, Take Heart) pulses an **aura** round its user in the move's colour, two rings rising up the body and four motes, under ten quads and gone within 0.8 s; a **protection** shows what it is made of (Spiky Shield thorns, green shards round the user's feet; Burning Bulwark embers; Shelter glints); a weather move is a light alone (Rain Dance a rain's grey-blue, Sunny Day a warm gold), the weather's own look being Y1's. Two reactions are mended: **cracks in a ring** (Earthquake, Thousand Arrows, Thousand Waves), four cracks spread to both sides of the target and before it, past its half width, so the punch-in sees them round the body and not behind it; and **blades** (Sacred Sword), the shards in pale steel, larger and with a white glint, so they read over the blade's own light.
  - **Y17f's batch** (the moves of our own past level 100, plan 06 · R31, which still play their type's template): staged by the same rules and the same parts, nothing new. A blow of 90 power or more is punched in on, a lance, pulse or current followed, a song, gale or fall of stones round the foes orbits, and a capstone blast wound up (Sunforge Blast, Mindbreak Wave, Permafrost Lance) is pushed in on and heaves or rocks its user; a body flung in lunges (Titan Tackle, Riptide Slam, Maelstrom Crash, Arc Lance, Glacier Ram, Serpent Crash), a dive from the sky rises and comes down (Talon Dive, Zenith Plunge), a blow from above presses, the ground's two jolt with cracks in a ring, the vein of magma tosses, a bite only shudders. A setup on its own side pulses Y17e's aura in its type's colour (Alloy Plating glints, Overcharge sparks) with no shot. The ordinary-strength ones (the priority jabs, the drains, Rivet Shot, Root Snare, Pilfer Strike, Sap Lance, Swarm Chorus) and the debuffs cast on the foes stay ordinary moves, as Tackle is.

  - **Y17g, the weather and the terrains spreading** (Rain Dance, Sunny Day, Sandstorm, Hail, Snowscape, Chilly Reception and the four terrains): the move starts at its user, as its own look has it, and then **spreads over the field** (`Directions.Spreads`): from just before the impact three puffs of the weather's colour (cloud for rain, a warm glow for sun, dust for sand, a pale cloud for hail and snow) glide from over the user's platform to over the foes' (`FxCtx.Foe`) and settle there, with a few flecks falling from them at the end; a terrain's is five glows low over the ground running from the user's feet to the foes' feet. Under ten quads, no shot, no stop, gone by the cue's end; the weather's lasting look stays Y1's and the terrain's Y2's.
  - **Limits**: what a direction lays over a recipe stays under `MoveFx.MaxQuads` with it, flashes at most 0.35 and shakes at most 0.5; no pass of its own; the stage light composes over the move's own (Y16), never over the Pokémon.
- **Colours by type**: Fire `255,128,36`, Water `64,146,255`, Electric `255,222,50`, Grass `96,196,72`, Ice `140,218,255`, Fighting `236,110,50`, Poison `172,78,210`, Ground `206,164,92`, Flying `176,206,255`, Psychic `255,92,176`, Bug `168,204,40`, Rock `184,158,110`, Ghost `132,92,210`, Dragon `112,96,250`, Dark `110,80,120`, Steel `196,208,228`, Fairy `255,150,214`, Normal `250,246,226`; each with a light colour for cores and sparks and a dark one for matter in shade.
- **Timing**: the impact lands 0.35 s in, with the damage; effects end by 1.3 s. A miss flies wide and past and lands nothing; a move that does nothing ends in a puff of smoke; a critical hit lands bigger with a white flash and a ring; a super-effective hit lands bigger with a second ring and a harder shake.
- **Screen flashes** at most 0.5 strong and under half a second (Thunder, Hyper Beam, Fire Blast, Blizzard, critical hits, Explosion); Sunny Day's warm glow is softer and longer.
- **Reduce flashes** (plan 12 · Q9, the options' REDUCE FLASHES, `Accessibility.Flash`): no flash over the picture is more than a quarter (`Accessibility.FlashCap`, 0.25): a move's (`FxList.ScreenFlash`), a ball's on the body it lets out, the evolution's and the hatching's, the field's lightning, a scene effect's burst, poison's purple in the field and a band's white. The two flashes that begin every way into a battle become one soft swell to a quarter and back over the same quarter of a second.
- **With the scene off** (plan 08 · P1, the options' Battle Scene; `BattleEngine.Scene`): as Platinum's switch has it (`BattleSystem_AreAnimationsOn`), nothing on this list plays but Transform's shape. No lunge and no doll stepping aside; no move's effect, no attack sent ahead landing; no stat's streaks, no heal's motes, no burst of a new status, none of the turn's moments, no weather's cue, no dodge, no cheer and no charge's crouch or aura (plan 25 · Y5: a charge's crouch goes with the lunge it stands in for); and none of their sounds. Tiredness stays: it is a state, as a condition is. What stays: the move's own sound, a hit's flicker, shake and sound (a critical hit's flash and ring are its effect's, and go with it), every send-out, recall, faint and ball, the Substitute's doll and its swaps, the weather's layer (it eases in with no cue), what lies on the field and what lasts on a Pokémon. What a line carries lands as the line appears, not 0.35 s in, so the HP bar drains with the text; the lines and their order are the rules' either way.
- **Marks on a Pokémon**: a stat rising sends warm streaks up round it, a stat falling cool streaks down; a heal is green motes and rings rising; a new status shows as flames (burn), purple bubbles (poison), crackling bolts (paralysis), drifting Z's (sleep) or a burst of ice (freeze).
  - **The turn's moments** (plan 25 · Y3; `MoveFx.Moments.cs`): what the end of a turn and the checks before a move do to a Pokémon, each a mark of its own, so the turn can be read from the picture: who is poisoned, who is seeded and by whom, which item went off. The list is the original's battle animations that are not moves (`BATTLE_ANIMATION_…`), and each is named after its original; **every look is our own**. A moment plays on the line that tells of it (the log's `MomentShown`), from the overview, for 1.1 s; it lands 0.35 s in, like a move, and a moment that hurts or heals **lands its HP then**: the bar drains or fills with no flinch of the body, no shake of the camera, no sound of a hit and no green aura of a heal, as the original skips the sprite's blink after one (`SYSCTL_SKIP_SPRITE_BLINK`). None flashes the screen: the turn's end is several of them in a row. Sizes are in the Pokémon's height *h*.
    - **Read from the overview, on any body.** Three rules the first pictures taught:
      - *A cast is matter.* The tint a moment lays over a body as it bites (purple for poison, pale blue for ice, red for a temper) is alpha-blended in its own colour: light added to a brown body only brightens it.
      - *What goes round a body is split at its middle.* The camera stands on the +Z side of the field, so whatever circles a Pokémon is drawn over its body while on that side of it and depth-tested behind it: flames, rocks, drops and grains whirl round a body of any width, where depth-testing all of them hid every one that a round body's own bulk covered. A band is three arcs for the same reason: the one toward the camera over the body, lit; the two behind hidden by it, and at half strength and unlit where a body narrower than the band lets them show, so they read as behind.
      - *Nothing lies flat for the far platform.* The lens sees the foe's platform at about 7°, so a ring or a swirl lying on the ground there is a thread. A band dips toward the camera (its plane tilted 29°), and a whirl on the ground has two more standing up in it, facing the lens, low round the legs.
    - *As each plays on its turn*: **asleep**, three Z's (`210,226,255`) drifting up from its head over a slow breath of pale light; **poisoned**, nine purple bubbles (0.07 to 0.11 *h*) rising through it that burst into rings as the poison bites, under a purple cast; **burned**, eight flames licking up its body and embers thrown off as the burn bites; **frozen**, eight shards of ice closing in round it and locking with a small glint under a pale blue cast; **paralysed**, three fits of bolts across its body with sparks jumping off; **confused**, three yellow stars (`255,226,90`) circling over its head under a turning swirl (the same mark as it becomes confused); **infatuated**, four pink hearts (`255,120,170`) rising from its head, swaying.
    - *What the turn's end takes or gives*: **curse**, a dark nail (`96,60,140`) driven down into it from above, wisps and a ring thrown out as it strikes; **nightmare**, a dark cloud of three puffs (`34,22,52`) over its head, six wisps circling it and closing in, and two red points of light in the cloud as it bites; **Leech Seed**, three leaves sprouting at its feet and twelve motes (a pale core in a green halo, `120,255,110`) leaving its body one after another for the Pokémon in the place the seed was sown from, in a low arc, with a glint where they arrive; **Ingrain**, six brown roots (`176,130,80` and `110,76,44`) growing in over the ground from 0.85 *h* out and up into its feet, lights running along them, while two rings close on it over the ground and sparks rise through a green cast.
    - *The seven binds*, each its move's own grip: **Bind**, two tan bands (`214,186,140`) tightening round its body, holding a beat and letting go, with a few stars as they bite; **Wrap**, three green coils (`120,170,80`) tilted one against the next; **Fire Spin**, a ring of fire on the ground and eighteen flames whirling round it and rising; **Magma Storm**, glowing rocks and flames whirling wider, embers thrown up from a red glow on the ground; **Clamp**, two pale arcs of shell (`186,206,226`) snapping shut on it from either side with a spark; **Whirlpool**, a blue swirl turning under it and two standing in it round its legs, drops running round its body and a splash as it bites; **Sand Tomb**, the same whirl in sand (`236,212,150`), drawing grains in and down round its legs under low dust.
    - *Items and a level*: **a held item** going off (where the original plays its held-item animation: Leftovers, a berry eaten, a Focus Sash, a Quick Claw…) shows just over its holder's head (1.1 *h*): a berry as a small round fruit (`236,84,110`, about a quarter of *h* across) with a dark leaf (`40,132,52`), popping up and bitten away into crumbs; anything else as a gold ring (`255,226,130`) closing on a glint. **A bag item** used on a Pokémon on the field is a glint over its head and pale motes (`214,244,255`) falling through it onto a ring at its feet. **An escape item** is a burst of pale smoke at the feet of the player's Pokémon, a second puff rolling up through it, and a few stars. **A level** is gold light (`255,232,140`) rising round the Pokémon: streaks, a ring climbing from its feet to its head and three stars popping over it, under the level's panel, which stands over the line.
    - *The Great Marsh*: **happy** (bait lands), three yellow notes hopping up from its head, each in a pale halo so it reads against leaves, with two sparkles; **eating**, a morsel of bait (`222,176,112`, rimmed `140,96,56`) over its head, where a held berry shows too (before its own body a morsel was lost on a brown one, and nobody can say where a body's mouth is), a third of it gone with each of three bites: a pale crescent snaps into it from one side and then the other, and pale crumbs fall from each; **angry** (mud lands), a red cast over its face, three puffs of steam off its head to either side and a jagged red mark (dark under bright) beating beside it.
    - **An attack sent ahead landing** (Future Sight, Doom Desire) plays its move's own cue in its landing half, from the place it was sent from (the log's `AheadLanded`): a glint there, then for Future Sight three rings of its type's light falling onto the target from that side and closing on it, for Doom Desire a pillar of pale light out of the sky with a flash of 0.25; then the move's own landing. The camera goes to the target at once: nobody need stand where it was sent from.
    - **What shows behind a doll** holds for these as it does for the other marks: none plays on a Pokémon behind a Substitute or out of sight, but the escape item's and a level's, and Leech Seed's plays only if both its ends can be seen. Each is a pure function of its cue's age and seed and draws at most 40 quads and ribbons (`MoveFx.MomentCap`). Sounds: *docs/sound-effects.md*, "Battles".
  - **A condition that lasts** (plan 09 · L2; `MoveFx.Conditions.cs`): a Pokémon that has a condition shows it for as long as it has it, not only as it is given and as it bites: at every menu, through every line, for turns on end. **Every look is our own**: between its moments Platinum shows a condition only as the letters on the HP box. So each is small and slow and keeps to the edge of the body or just over its head: it must never cover the Pokémon or its HP box, and it must read from the overview on both platforms. Sizes are in the Pokémon's height *h*.
    - *The body's own* (the pose and the shader; no quads): **asleep**, its eyes are shut; **frozen**, it is held still at one moment of its idle loop (no breath, no sway, no blink) under an ice-blue cast (`150,214,255`, 40 % over its own colours, shimmering by 4 %); **poisoned**, a purple cast (`172,78,210`) pulses over it, to 24 % and away over 0.9 s, every 2.4 s; **badly poisoned** deepens it, a darker violet (`128,48,176`) to 34 % every 1.5 s; **paralysed**, a yellow glint (`255,232,90`, 22 %) for a tenth of a second as each fit of sparks begins. A cast is blended into the model's own colours by the shader's flash, so it is matter and keeps its colour on any body (light added to a brown body only brightens it), and it gives way to the flash of a send-out, a recall and a wild Pokémon's silhouette.
    - *Beside this body*. A body's height says little of its width: a Bidoof is as wide as it is tall, an Empoleon a quarter as wide, and marks set out by height alone sat inside the one and floated a body's width off the other. So the battle measures each Pokémon as it is turned to the lens (`FxPlaces.HalfWidth`: half its silhouette's width *w*, from the sprite framing of that view, kept between a fifth and three fifths of its height so a tail or a pair of wings doesn't send things far off), and what stands beside a body stands off its middle by *w*: it hugs a narrow body and clears a wide one. What is over its head goes by *h*, and the ring round its head by both.
    - *The marks about it*, each a loop in its own age:
      - **asleep**: three Z's (`214,228,255`, each over a shadow of its own, `70,96,170`, a sixth of its size down and to the right, so a pale letter reads on a pale sky and on leaves) rising one after another from beside its head, up and to its right: from just outside the ring a confused head's stars keep to, at 1.0 *h*, to 0.26 *h* further right at 1.34 *h*, growing from 0.06 to 0.11 *h*, one every 0.8 s, each 2.4 s on its way.
      - **poisoned**: four bubbles (0.07 to 0.1 *h*: a purple body, `150,62,190` at three fifths' strength, under a pale rim and glint, since a bubble that is only its rim is lost on leaves) rising at the edge of its body, 0.65 to 1 *w* either side of its middle, from 0.3 to 1.1 *h*, wobbling, each 1.9 s on its way and gone at the top in a small ring. **Badly poisoned**: six, darker (`104,36,150` under a rim of `206,120,236`), each 1.3 s on its way.
      - **burned**: four small flames (0.16 to 0.2 *h* across their quads, the flame itself half of that: an orange body `255,128,36` that is matter, with a pale heart added) on its flanks (0.95 *w* out, at 0.42 and 0.5 *h*), a shoulder (0.62 *w*, 0.8 *h*) and its head (0.35 *w*, 0.99 *h*), each licking up 0.08 *h* and starting again twice a second, flickering.
      - **paralysed**: every 1.7 s a fit of a quarter of a second: two short crooked bolts across its body, 0.05 *h* wide (yellow matter `255,222,50` with a white core added), within 0.95 *w* of its middle and 0.42 *h* of its waist, and three sparks jumping off; nothing between fits.
      - **frozen**: a shell of ice: six shards (0.15 to 0.21 *h*) standing round its feet on a ring 1.05 *w* out, two more (0.14 *h*) leaning off its shoulders at 0.95 *w* and 0.58 *h*, pale blue (`140,218,255` and `236,252,255`) at four fifths' strength so the body shows through them; those behind it are hidden by it. A glint pops on one of them every 1.3 s.
      - **confused**: three yellow stars (`255,226,90` on a rim of `176,120,20`, 0.075 *h*) circling its head at 1.08 *h*, once round in 2.4 s, on a ring 0.8 *w* out (never under 0.2 nor over 0.32 *h*) and two fifths as deep; behind the head they are hidden by it.
      - **infatuated**: two pink hearts (`255,120,170` on a rim of `196,60,120`, 0.07 to 0.095 *h*) rising one after the other from over its head, to its left: 0.1 to 0.26 *h* left of its middle, from 1.1 to 1.34 *h*, swaying, each 2 s on its way.
      - **salt cured** (plan 06 · R26b, Salt Cure): three grains of salt (shards, 0.07 to 0.09 *h*, a pale grey-white body `236,232,222` over a darker rim `150,142,128` a fifth bigger) clinging low at the edge of its body, at 0.85 to 1 *w* out and 0.2 to 0.5 *h* up, each slowly turning; a glint (`255,255,255`) pops on one every 1.6 s.
      - **a locked heart** (plan 44 · SD2; the user's answer 63a, grey and drifting): four grey wisps (smoke, `118,116,128`, 0.16 to 0.24 *h*, at seven tenths' strength, alpha-blended so the grey keeps its colour on any ground) drifting slowly up its flanks, 1.05 to 1.3 *w* out from its middle, one wisp every 0.8 s on a 3.2 s loop, never over its middle. While it is **loose** two darker wisps (`72,68,84`) close in at 0.8 of that and a dull pulse of the same dark (a glow 1.6 *w* across, at most a third's strength) beats at its feet six times a second. Seven quads at most (`MoveFx.HazeCap`). On the HP box and the party's cards a **padlock** (`ModernUi.LockMark`: a body `118,116,128` on a rim `64,60,76`, a shackle ring over it, a keyhole) stands after the name where a shiny's star would (after the star if both). The summary shows the lock's **gauge** where the EXP bar is (five rounded bars side by side, full ones grey, emptied ones the pale track, the last one's share drawn), with the EXP held under it, and a **sealed move** is a row washed grey with a padlock where its numbers are. **CALM** stands where RUN does while the Pokémon there is loose, as SHIFT does in a triple. **The opening** (`UI/HeartOpeningScreen.cs`): on the evolution scene's stage the Pokémon stands under its haze (six soft grey lights circling it, the body cast grey at under half), 1.6 s; the haze lifts over 1.4 s in the evolution's aura, its cry, then the line, and the level panel for the EXP held, two presses as the bag's Rare Candy's.
      - **covered in syrup** (plan 06 · R26b, Syrup Bomb): three drops of syrup (0.06 to 0.08 *h*: amber matter `214,120,40` with a pale shine `255,214,140` a third its size added at the top of the first) gathering on its shoulders, 0.6 to 0.9 *w* either side at 0.7 to 0.85 *h*, swelling and sliding down 0.3 *h* to drip off, each 2.2 s on its way.
    - *Sharing the Pokémon*. A condition, confusion and love are three marks and can show at once: the Z's keep to its right, the hearts to its left, the stars round its head. **A moment or a mark given plays alone**: while a turn's moment, a condition being given, a stat's change or a heal plays on a Pokémon, the marks about it stand back (out over 0.15 s, back over the cue's last quarter of a second), and the body's own (the shut eyes, the hold, the cast) stays. So poison's bite is the moment's nine bubbles and their rings, and afterwards the four go on rising; a Pokémon put to sleep shows the drifting Z's of its falling asleep, and then the three that last.
    - *Coming and going*. A mark eases in over 0.4 s from the line that tells of the condition and out over 0.4 s on the line that says it is over: a cure, waking, a thaw, snapping out of confusion, the one loved leaving the field. A faint and a recall take it with the Pokémon. A Pokémon that comes in with a condition (asleep on the bench, poisoned before the battle) shows it from the line that sends it out, once it is out of its ball; a confusion handed on by Baton Pass comes in the same way.
    - *What isn't shown*: no mark about a Pokémon behind a Substitute's doll, out of sight in the middle of a move, still in its ball, fainting, being called back or drawn into a ball, as for every other mark on a Pokémon (the original's `BattleSystem_ShouldShowStatusEffect`). The body's own shows wherever the body does.
    - The looks are set by the log's events (`StatusChanged`, `VolatileChanged`, `CameInWith`, a send-out, a recall, a faint), never read from the rules, and brought level with them when a menu opens, with nothing played. Each is a pure function of its age, how far it has come in and a seed from the looks' own count, and none flashes the screen or shakes the camera. Caps, in quads and ribbons (`MoveFx.ConditionCap`): asleep 6, poisoned 8, badly poisoned 12, burned 8, paralysed 7, frozen 10, confused 6, infatuated 4; a Pokémon with a condition, confused and in love at once draws 22 at the most, against a moment's 40. The later moves' marks (`MarkChanged`, `MoveMark`) count apart (`MoveFx.SaltCap`, `SyrupCap`): salt cured 7, covered in syrup 4; the salt keeps low and the syrup to the shoulders, so neither crosses the Z's, the bubbles or the stars.
- **The stage's light** (plan 25 · Y16; `Graphics/StageLight.cs`). Where the original fades the battle's background toward a colour (`Func_FadeBg`), switches it for a picture of its own (`SwitchBg`) or turns it grey (`Func_SetBgGrayscale`), the stage alone changes its light: the arena, its sky, its platforms, its own lights and a trainer still standing on it are washed toward a colour by an amount and drawn toward their grey, and **the Pokémon, the dolls, the balls and every effect keep the scene's own light**. A full-screen flash would dim the Pokémon too and is never used for it.
  - *The vocabulary* is one `StageLight` (a colour, an amount 0..1, a grey 0..1) to a move (`StageLights.Table`, 162 moves). A fade to *n*/16 is an amount of *n*/16 toward our colour of the original's name (black `5,5,13`, dark red `92,13,8`, light blue `153,204,255`, light green `140,230,128`, pale yellow `255,240,140`, purple `77,20,107`…); a switched picture, which is art, is a wash in a colour of the move's own (Thunder a storm's navy, Fire Blast a dark red, Psychic a pink-violet, Trick Room a violet), mostly at 0.6; a grey stage is a grey of 1 with 0.15 of black.
  - *Caps*: no wash goes past **0.7** (`MostDark`), and none toward a pale colour (its luma over 0.8) past **0.55** (`MostPale`): a Pokémon is always read against its stage, and a white stage would bloom over it.
  - *Timing*: it comes in over 0.18 s from the cue's start (eased), holds, and is gone 0.05 s before the cue ends, going over 0.35 s. A move's charge half, an attack sent ahead landing, a Z-Move's and a Max Move's set pieces leave the stage as it is; a miss doesn't (the original fades it whatever the move does). Two at once lay one wash over the other. With the battle's scene off nothing plays.
  - *Cost*: two uniforms (`stageWash`, `stageGrey`) in the lit programs' fog step, set before the stage and put back to nothing before the Pokémon; no pass of its own. The field never sets them.
- **Bodies that move with a move** (plan 25 · Y16; `Graphics/BodyMotion.cs`). What the original does with the sprite itself is the body's here, in its own height so it reads alike on both platforms: a **rush** to 0.8 of its height toward the foe at the impact and back (Take Down, Double-Edge, Giga Impact, Flare Blitz, Brave Bird, Volt Tackle, Head Smash, Skull Bash, Superpower, Aqua Tail, Reversal, U-turn; Gyro Ball and Double-Edge turning once as they go), a **leap** in an arc 0.3 high (Jump Kick, High Jump Kick, Rolling Kick), a **spin** (Thrash twice, Petal Dance three times), a **rise** of 0.35 (Waterfall), a **lift** of 0.35 held through the cue (Magnet Rise), a **puff**, pressed and springing three times by 0.14 (Belly Drum, Slack Off, Bulk Up, Power Trick), a **squeeze** of its target's width by up to 0.16, three times (Wrap, Bind, Crush Grip, only on a hit), Gravity's **press** of every body by 0.14, and a **collapse** to 0.6 of its size, sunk 0.2, which a spent user keeps until it faints (Memento, Final Gambit, Healing Wish, Lunar Dance). A body that goes **out of sight** for a two-turn move is drawn going over 0.5 s, the way its move goes (up four heights for Fly, Bounce and Sky Drop; into the platform for Dig and Dive; shrinking to nothing for Shadow Force and Phantom Force), and coming back the same way over 0.35 s on its strike's line. With nothing playing a body stands exactly where it stood (the matrices are the same to the bit). No motion shakes the camera; the shapes stay within 0.8 to 1.2 of the body's own.
- **A body's tint** (plan 25 · Y17g, Y16's owed table; `Graphics/BodyTint.cs`, GPU-free). Where the original fades a battler's sprite toward a colour (`Func_FadeBattlerSprite`, 153 moves), the body is tinted through the shader's flash, blended into its own colours: on its **user** from the cue's start (Dragon Dance's red, Recover's pale yellow, Shadow Punch's black), on its **target** from the impact and only when the move landed (Surf's teal, Thunderbolt's dark jolt, Sing's pink). A tint is our colour of the original's name at `BodyTints.Most` (0.45) × *n*/16 of its strength, a white one no further than `MostPale` (0.3), so the body is never lost in it; it comes in over 0.12 s, holds, and is gone 0.4 s after the impact for a target, by the cue's end for a user. It gives way to a send-out's or recall's flash, a change of shape's light, a giant's cast and a silhouette, and over a lasting condition's cast the stronger shows. With the Battle Scene off no cue plays, so no tint.

### Poké Ball (G8)

- **A 3D model** (`BattleBall`): two hollow halves sculpted with the SDF kit, hinged at the back, with a dark band round the seam and a white button in a dark ring at the front, glossy (`Plastic`), outlined like the Pokémon. Kinds by colour: Poké Ball red over white; Great Ball blue with red patches either side; Ultra Ball black with a yellow H; Master Ball purple with two pink domes and a white M; Premier Ball white with a red band; the others in their own colours with a stripe or spots.
- **Thrown by the trainer**, overarm (the `Throw` emote lets go 0.3 s in). At a send-out the trainer throws it onto the platform and steps aside; it opens in a white burst and the Pokémon grows out of the light. Later send-outs come in from the trainer's side of the field.
- **A capture**: the player runs in at the left of their platform and throws; the ball spins end over end along a high arc, pops open over the foe and draws it in with red light, shuts, drops onto the platform with a bounce, wobbles once per successful shake check with a pause after each, and then clicks shut with three stars (it stays on the platform) or bursts open in white light as the Pokémon breaks out.
- **A critical capture** (plan 40 · GE5, the modern rules' `Ruleset.CriticalCaptures`; `BallPhase.Rattle`, `BattleAnimator.BallRattleTime` 0.5 s): as the ball shuts over the foe it hangs where it shut and rattles twice in the air (a rock to either side and back, twice, easing a third smaller by its end), with a white glint at the button on the camera's side and a pale ring of light (`255,248,210`, at most 150 of alpha) swelling round it, eased in over its first 0.15 and out after 0.6 of it; the `ball_critical` sound rings as it begins. Then it drops as any ball does, wobbles **once**, whichever way it goes, and clicks shut or bursts. The camera keeps its close shot of the ball through the rattle, as through the opening and the drop.

- **Each its own ball** (plan 25 · Y4; plan 20 · J6 added Johto's seven). A Pokémon comes out of, and goes back into, the ball it was caught in (`Pokemon.Ball`); one never caught, a trainer's, an Egg's hatchling or a ball the model has no kind for (the Park Ball) is in a Poké Ball, as Platinum makes a trainer's (plan 25, decision 5). The model has twenty-three kinds: the fifteen above, the Cherish Ball, red over black with a gold band and gold stripe (`200,40,50`, `50,40,50`, `240,200,80`), and Johto's seven Apricorn balls (plan 20 · J6, our own looks): the Fast Ball yellow `244,190,50` with red wings `226,54,60`; the Level Ball red with three rising yellow bars on the front; the Lure Ball blue `60,130,210` with a red stripe; the Heavy Ball slate `84,92,112` over a darker bottom `60,66,82` with a pale stripe `190,196,210`; the Love Ball pink `244,130,170` with a white heart; the Friend Ball green `96,180,80` with five yellow spots `250,232,120`; the Moon Ball deep blue `48,56,120` over a pale bottom `224,228,244` with a yellow crescent `250,232,140`.
- **The release** (`BattleBall.ReleaseOf`, `BallRelease.Emit`): every ball opens in the flash, ring and fourteen sparks the Poké Ball has always had (white `255,255,255`, light `255,246,200`: unchanged for it), tinted from its look, with matter of its own added, at most 28 quads more, all gone by 0.6 s after the lid opens:
  | Ball | Flash / light | Its own |
  |---|---|---|
  | Poké | white / cream | nothing |
  | Great | pale blue / blue | blue sparks flung out as streaks |
  | Ultra | pale gold / gold | gold stars and a few puffs of black smoke |
  | Master | lilac / violet | a violet swirl ring and pink stars |
  | Premier | white / red | a second, red ring and falling white feathers |
  | Safari | pale green / green | green leaves falling |
  | Net | teal / deep blue | a lattice of crosses spreading out |
  | Dive | pale blue / sea blue | bubbles rising |
  | Nest | yellow-green / green | yellow and green leaves drifting down |
  | Repeat | orange / yellow | two more rings, each after the last |
  | Timer | white / red | two slow rings and ticking dots |
  | Luxury | gold / gold | gold motes rising and glints |
  | Dusk | violet / orange | dark motes rising and one orange glint |
  | Heal | pink / white | hearts rising |
  | Quick | yellow / blue | fast streaks flung out |
  | Cherish | red / gold | red petals and white stars |
  | Fast | pale yellow / gold | ten gold streaks flung out fast |
  | Level | pale red / red | three rings, each wider than the last, the last yellow |
  | Lure | pale blue / blue | bubbles rising and a few red sparks |
  | Heavy | grey / slate | dust thrown low and falling back, a slate ring |
  | Love | pale pink / pink | hearts rising |
  | Friend | pale green / green | green and yellow leaves and a few yellow stars |
  | Moon | pale violet / violet-blue | yellow stars drifting down and a pale glow over the head |
  The release is a pure function of the ball's look, the time since the lid opened and a seed. A Pokémon that breaks out of a thrown ball bursts in that ball's release and keeps that ball.
- **The recall** (`BallRecall`, 0.9 s, `BattleAnimator.RecallTime`): the Pokémon's own ball flies in from where the next send-out would come from (the far side of its platform, the trainer having stepped off), growing, to a little over the Pokémon's middle; the lid swings open; a red beam of three ribbons and a red glow draw the Pokémon in as it shrinks (over the middle third); the lid shuts; the ball flies out the way it came, shrinking, and is off the field by the end. It then takes the Pokémon's ball's look, so a Luxury Ball takes it back in gold and black.

### Weather in battle (plan 25 · Y1)

- **What lasts while the weather does** (`ArenaFx.Weather`), eased in over a second as the weather begins and out over a second as it stops, in the scene and behind whatever stands in front of it:
  - *Rain*: thin streaks falling steeply, slanted a little toward the camera, rings spreading where drops land on both platforms and on the ground between them, and a faint grey veil over the far field.
  - *Harsh sun*: warm motes drifting up through the light, soft haze over the platforms and a glare in the upper left of the sky.
  - *Sandstorm*: streaks blown across the field from left to right, low and fast, with clouds of dust rolling through them.
  - *Hail*: stones falling fast and bouncing once off the platforms and the ground, small and white with a blue cast.
  - *Fog*: three low layers of soft cloud drifting at different speeds, thickest at the platforms' height.
- **The cue** (`ArenaFx.WeatherCue`, 1.2 s) plays over the layer as the weather begins and again on each turn it goes on (the turn's line, "Rain continues to fall.", carries the log's `WeatherContinues`, as the original plays the weather's animation every turn): a heavier shower splashing on both platforms; a warm flash with rays slanting down; a wall of sand sweeping across; a flurry of hail; fog rolling in from the left.
- **The light** (`ArtLook.InBattleWeather`): rain, hail, a sandstorm and fog take the light of the field's weather they come from (`Weathers.FromBattle`, the way back from `Weathers.InBattle`: rain as the field's rain, hail as its hail), and the sky is drawn toward a grey of its own brightness (rain and hail cool, the sandstorm ochre, fog pale) with the stars hidden. The harsh sun, which the field has no weather for, has a rig of its own (`ArtLook.HarshSun`): the key 34 % stronger and warmer, warm light from the sky and ground, bloom +0.18 on a threshold 0.08 lower, a paler, warmer sky. A weather clearing and the next coming in are blended by how far each has come.
- **The later games' weathers** (plan 06 · R19b), our own looks like the rest:
  - *Snow* (hail's place under the modern rules): white flakes (`246,250,255`) 0.08 to 0.15 units across drifting down slowly through rain's box, each swaying as it falls and never bouncing; its cue is a quicker flurry of them; its light is hail's, its sound hail's.
  - *Extremely harsh sun*: the harsh sun's layer with a second, whiter glare over the sun's and a pale warm wash breathing over the far ground; its light, cue and sound are the harsh sun's.
  - *Heavy rain*: rain's layer twice over, the second a little fainter and out of step; its light hides more of the sky (85 %) than rain's; its cue and sound are rain's.
  - *Strong winds*: long pale gusts (`236,246,252`) streaming across the field from the left at every height the lens takes in, with a few wisps curling as they go; the sky only thinned (35 %); its sound is a sandstorm's.
- **Sounds**: each weather has its own as it begins and as it goes on (`weather_rain`, `weather_sun`, `weather_sandstorm`, `weather_hail`, `weather_fog`; *docs/sound-effects.md*).
- Everything is a pure function of the weather's age, the battle's clock and the cue's seed: nothing is simulated.
- **A streak must be wide enough to see** (plan 25 · Y2, the first session to look at these on a screen): the streak's shape shows a line about a sixth of its quad's width, so at their first numbers rain was under a pixel wide at 1080p and a sandstorm a dozen threads. Rain's streaks are drawn 2.5 times those widths and a sandstorm's and hail's twice, each at its own length (`ArenaFx.WeatherStreak`): rain about two to four pixels wide near the camera, sand three to six. And they fall **where the lens looks**: rain in a box of 23 by 6 by 33 units from the camera to the far trees (`ArenaFx.RainMin`, `RainMax`), a sandstorm's sand in one of 26 by 4.5 by 32, where the sky's whole box is four times the rain's and left a handful of streaks in the picture.

### The Substitute (plan 25 · Y1)

- **The doll** (`SubstituteDoll`) is our own small figure, not the original's: a plush doll of undyed cloth (`226,210,176`, its feet and ears a shade darker) with a round head, two nubs of ears, stub arms, button eyes, two cheeks of thread, a patch on its belly (`196,118,92`) and a seam stitched down its front and over its head. It is sculpted with the SDF kit in `Cloth`, meshed once and cached like the Poké Ball, preloaded with it, and outlined like the Pokémon. It stands three quarters of its platform's radius tall (times the double battle's fit), a little in front of its Pokémon's spot, facing the other side.
- **Up** (0.7 s): the Pokémon shrinks out of sight over the first 45 % while the doll falls from above and lands with a squash and a ring of dust.
- **Standing**: it breathes (a slow squash of 3 %). A hit that lands on it makes it flinch: a tilt back and a jolt with a few stars (0.45 s). For its owner's own move it slides aside, the Pokémon grows back to make the move, and the two swap back once the move has played.
- **Down** (0.6 s): it whitens and shrinks into a puff of smoke while the Pokémon grows back. A doll passed by Baton Pass fades with the Pokémon that leaves and drops in again once the one that comes in is out of its ball.
- **What shows behind a doll** (the original's `BattleSystem_ShouldShowStatusEffect`): a mark on the Pokémon (a stat's change, a heal, a condition given) isn't played while a doll stands in front of it or it is out of sight; the weather's cue and a move's own effect, which lands on the doll, play as ever.
- The doll is put up and taken down by the log's lines (`SubstituteChanged`), never read from the rules; its looks are pure functions of its ages and the animator's seed. Sounds: `substitute_in`, `substitute_out`.

### What lies on the field (plan 25 · Y2)

Platinum draws a hazard, a screen or Trick Room only as its move is used, and afterwards only lines that have scrolled away tell of them. Here each is drawn for as long as it lasts. **All of these looks are our own**: none is the original's, and none is taken from a later game.

- **Small and low, never over a body.** Everything lies on a platform or the ground, stands low on its rim, or hangs above a Pokémon's head. On the arc of a rim that faces the camera, where a thing comes between the lens and the Pokémon, nothing stands higher than a ninth of the platform's radius (the panes, which reach the feet and no further); what stands taller (the stones, the charms, a puff of mist) keeps to the rest of the rim, 65° clear of the camera either way. All of it sits in the scene (depth-tested), so a body hides what is behind it. Sizes are in the platform's radius *R* (the player's 1.2, the foe's 2.4), so both sides read the same on screen.
- **Matter keeps its colour, light is added.** A caltrop, a stone, a pane's glass, the grid's lines, a star and a mote are alpha-blended in their own colour, because light alone washes out to white over a bright field; their edges, glints and halos are light added on top.
- **What lies in wait**, on the side it was scattered on:
  - *Spikes*: one ring of steel caltrops (`196,204,220`, their crossed point `120,128,150`) round the rim for each layer, at 0.95, 0.84 and 0.73 *R*, ten to a ring, each 0.06 *R* tall. A new layer falls from above, each caltrop in its own moment, and settles with a hop; fading, they sink into the ground.
  - *Toxic Spikes*: the same caltrops in purple (`186,120,236`, their crossed point `120,64,160`), in rings between Spikes' at 0.895 and 0.785 *R*, with bubbles of poison (`214,150,255`) rising from three of them at a time.
  - *Stealth Rock*: five pointed stones (`168,150,122`, a splinter of `196,180,150` standing out of each) hovering 0.22 to 0.39 *R* over the platform's top, just outside the rim at 1.08 *R*, on the flanks and the far side, each bobbing and turning on its own slow rhythm over a blot of shade.
- **Screens and veils**, on the side that put them up:
  - *Reflect*: a low pane of warm glass (`255,196,110`) along the 100° of the rim that face the other side, from the platform's top to 0.05 *R*, with a bright upper edge and a glint sliding along it. It rises from its foot and spreads from its middle as it comes in.
  - *Light Screen*: the same pane in cool glass (`140,214,255`), standing on Reflect's: from 0.055 to 0.105 *R*. With both up the two make one pane of two colours.
  - *Safeguard*: a pale green ring (`190,255,214`) lying round the platform's foot at 1.12 *R*, breathing slowly, with motes rising a hand's breadth from it. A veil is drawn as its foot: a dome over the Pokémon would cover it.
  - *Mist*: wisps of white mist lying flat round the rim in two thin layers that drift opposite ways, and four low puffs standing on the rim clear of the camera.
  - *Tailwind*: pale streaks of wind (`214,236,255`) running low along the ground past both flanks of the platform, from behind the side toward the other.
  - *Lucky Chant*: three small charms, pink stars (`255,186,224`), hovering just over the rim on the flanks and the far side, bobbing, each with a twinkle now and then.
- **The rooms**:
  - *Trick Room*: a grid of violet lines (`214,120,255`) on the ground under the whole field, seven each way, rising as walls 3.2 units high behind the foe's platform and at both sides, with two rails along each. No wall stands between the camera and the field. Each line is a line of the world from end to end (a ribbon, not a sprite turned to the camera), so the grid runs away from the lens as the ground does; the lines grow out from the middle as it comes in, and a wave passes along them while it lasts.
  - *Gravity*: the stage's light is lowered (`ArtLook.UnderGravity`: the key and both ambients to 84 %, the shadows drawn a little toward violet, the vignette 0.08 stronger), and short violet-grey streaks (`170,150,210`) sink to the ground round both platforms, faster as they fall.
  - *Wonder Room* (plan 06 · R25): on both platforms three small hexagonal shields (`Hex`) hover over the rim on the flanks and the far side, 0.26 to 0.38 *R* over the top at 1.06 *R*, each turning over every two seconds, teal (`110,230,220`) on one face and indigo (`130,120,255`) on the other: what guards and what is guarded, swapped. They keep 65° clear of the camera, as the stones do, and rise into place as it comes in.
  - *Magic Room* (plan 06 · R25): on both platforms three pink motes (`Orb`, `236,140,214`) hover in the same places, dimming and brightening slowly: the held items' power, put away.
- **Sent ahead**, over the place it waits for:
  - *A Wish*: a small gold star (`255,226,110`) in a soft halo over the head of whoever stands there, a little to the left, bobbing, its rays turning slowly.
  - *Future Sight*: a mote of violet (`214,96,240`) beside the star's place, circling on the spot, a ring leaving it every second and a half. *Doom Desire* is the same in pale steel (`206,214,236`) with a cross of light.
- **The later games'** (plan 06 · R19b):
  - *Aurora Veil*: two curtains of the northern lights, green (`120,255,196`) under pink (`236,150,255`), hanging over the rim on the flanks and the far side, 0.3 and 0.46 *R* over the top and 0.42 and 0.3 *R* deep, rippling, with glints running down them; they rise into being as it comes in. Like the stones, they keep 65° clear of the camera.
  - *Sticky Web*: a web of silver threads (`226,230,240`) lying just over the platform's top, eight spokes out to the rim and two rings sagging between them at 0.42 and 0.78 *R*, dew glinting along a thread now and then. A body hides what is under it; the spokes run out from the middle as it comes in.
  - *A terrain*, on both platforms at once, one terrain at a time: a wash of its colour lying on the top and a low band of it standing round the far rim (0.12 *R* deep: a wash alone is a thread on the foe's platform), and its own pieces on the far rim. *Electric* (`255,236,110`): sparks leaping for a moment along the ground. *Grassy* (`96,196,92`): blades of grass swaying. *Misty* (`255,176,226`): puffs of pink mist drifting round, with sparkles rising. *Psychic* (`196,110,255`): rings spreading over the top and violet motes rising from the rim.
  - *What two Pledges leave* (plan 06 · R25's leftovers), each for four turns:
    - *A rainbow*, over the side whose Water and Fire Pledges met: five thin bands of colour (`255,120,120`, `255,184,96`, `255,236,120`, `128,220,136`, `128,170,255`, the outermost red and highest) arching over the rim on the flanks and the far side from end to end of the arc that faces nobody, 0.2 *R* over the top at its feet and 0.62 *R* at its crown, a little outside the rim; alpha-blended, so they keep their colour over a bright sky, shimmering slowly band by band. They rise from their feet as it comes in.
    - *A sea of fire*, round the side the Fire and Grass Pledges struck: an ember-red wash (`232,84,40`) lying on the platform's top and a low band of it standing round the far rim, as a terrain's (0.12 *R* deep), with six tongues of flame (`Flame`, `255,150,60`) licking up from the far rim, each flickering on its own beat, 0.1 to 0.2 *R* tall.
    - *A swamp*, round the side the Grass and Water Pledges struck: a muddy olive wash (`112,120,64`) on the top, the same low band on the far rim in a darker mud (`88,96,52`), and three bubbles (`196,206,150`, each on a darker body) rising slowly from the far rim and bursting.
  - *What G-Max Moves leave* (plan 06 · R22; stand-ins until plan 25 · Y15 gives Dynamax its looks):
    - *G-Max Steelsurge's steel*, on the side it was scattered on until Defog, Rapid Spin or G-Max Wind Rage blows it away: five splinters of steel (`Shard`, `176,188,206`, a pale glint `236,242,250` on each) hovering over the rim as Stealth Rock's stones do, 0.24 to 0.38 *R* over the top, turning slowly, each over a faint shadow; they keep 65° clear of the camera and rise one after another as it comes in.
    - *G-Max Wildfire, Cannonade, Vine Lash and Volcalith*, round the other side for four turns, each as a Pledge's sea of fire is: a wash of its colour on the top and the same low band on the far rim (0.12 *R* deep), with six of its own pieces along the far rim on their own beats. *Wildfire*: flames (`Flame`) in a hotter orange than the sea of fire's (`255,120,40` on `236,96,32`). *Cannonade*: drops of water (`Drop`, `120,184,255` on `64,128,220`) falling onto the rim. *Vine Lash*: leaves (`Leaf`, `112,212,96` on `64,150,72`) whipping to and fro. *Volcalith*: glowing rocks (`Rock`, `255,150,90` on `150,88,64`) bobbing on the rim.
  - The whole field with every kind up at once, Aurora Veil, Sticky Web, the three Pledges' and the five G-Max Moves' on both sides and the heaviest terrain among them, draws at most 627 quads and ribbons with Wonder Room and Magic Room up too (`BattleFieldFx.MaxQuads`).
- **Coming and going.** A look comes in over 0.6 s from the line that tells of it (the move's own effect plays on the line before) and fades over 0.5 s on the line that says it is gone: a screen wearing off, Brick Break, Defog, Rapid Spin, a Poison type taking up the Toxic Spikes, a Wish coming true, an attack sent ahead landing. Where the rules drop a Wish or an attack sent ahead with no line (nobody stands in its place), an event between lines takes it down.
- The looks are set by the log's events (`SideConditionChanged`, `FieldConditionChanged`, `PlaceConditionChanged`), never read from the rules, and brought level with them when a menu opens; each is a pure function of its ages, the battle's clock and a seed from the animator, and none draws more than its cap of quads (`BattleFieldFx.Cap`). No pass and no render target is added: the panes and the grid are quads and ribbons of the effects' own atlas.

### Mega Evolution, Ultra Burst and Z-Moves (plan 25 · Y14)

The later games' changes of shape and their strongest moves, as the rules of plan 06 · R20 and R21 tell of them (`MegaEvolved`, `UltraBursted`, `ZPowerUsed`, and a Z-Move's own `MoveShown`). **Every look here is our own**: none is taken from a later game's art, and their beats (when the light gathers, when the shape changes, when a Z-Move lands) are ours too, since Platinum has none of it. They are cues like a move's (`MoveFx.Later.cs`, `MoveFx.ZMoves.cs`): pure functions of the cue's age and seed, drawn in the 3D pass, each under `MoveFx.MaxQuads` (124) at every age.

- **Nothing hides the Pokémon, nothing shakes harder than a hit.** No screen flash is over 0.35 or longer than a third of a second; a Z-Move's extra shake is at most 0.5 (Earthquake's is 0.35, a hit's own jolt is the animator's); nothing stands between the lens and a body for longer than its landing. What circles a body is split at its middle as the turn's moments are (the half on the camera's side drawn over it, the rest depth-tested), and nothing is drawn higher than 1.3 *h* over a Pokémon's feet, which is what the overview takes in above the foe.
- **Mega Evolution** (`CueKind.Mega`, 1.6 s, `BattleAnimator.MegaTime`), on the line "X has Mega Evolved into Mega X!":
  - *Gathering* (0 to 0.8 s): four ribbons of light (violet `186,96,255`, pink `255,120,200`, orange `255,176,70`, cyan `110,220,255`, each a body of its colour at 0.7 under a white core added) spiral up round the body on a helix 0.75 *w* out, from the feet to 1.15 *h*, two turns each, drawing tighter (to 0.5 *w*) as they rise; twelve motes of the four colours rise with them; a ring of the same light lies round its feet and narrows.
  - *The body*: the shader's flash whitens it (`MoveFx.BodyCast`: white `255,252,240`, from 0.3 s to the whole body by 0.72 s, held to 0.92 s, gone by 1.3 s): a silhouette of light. **Its shape changes inside the light**, 0.8 s in (`BattleAnimator.MegaSwapTime`): the screen's Pokémon takes the Mega's form then, never as the line appears, so the light that goes in is the old shape and the light that comes out the new.
  - *The burst* (0.8 s): a ring facing the lens grows to 1.5 *h* in the four colours, sixteen sparks are thrown out, and the Mega mark of our own (a ring 0.42 *h* across over the head at 1.15 *h*, a four-pointed star in its middle and the four colours turning round it) stands for 0.6 s and fades; a screen flash of pale violet, 0.3 at its height, over 0.25 s.
  - *Settling* (to 1.6 s): sparks of the four colours drift down round the new body and go out.
  - **Primal Reversion** is the same in the Pokémon's own colour: Kyogre's blue (`64,146,255`, `190,232,255`) with drops thrown out in place of sparks, Groudon's red (`236,70,40`, `255,200,110`) with embers; its mark is a ring with three points of its colour (`Star` turned), no star in the four colours. The cue says which by the type the rules give the form (Water or Ground); sound `primal_reversion`.
- **Ultra Burst** (`CueKind.UltraBurst`, 1.8 s, `BattleAnimator.UltraBurstTime`), Necrozma's: the body darkens (the flash at `40,26,70`, to 0.85 by 0.5 s) while eight prisms (`Shard`, gold `255,214,110` over a dark violet body `70,40,120`) close in round it from 1.1 *w* to its edge; from 0.5 s cracks of light (gold ribbons with a white core, 0.03 *h* wide) run out of its middle, more and longer; at 0.85 s (`UltraBurstSwapTime`) the body turns to light (white-gold `255,244,200`, held to 1.05 s, gone by 1.45 s) and its shape changes inside it, and eight rays of gold (ribbons 0.07 *h* wide, out to 1.3 *h*) burst from its middle with a ring and a screen flash of pale gold 0.35; gold dust settles to 1.8 s. Sound `ultra_burst`.
- **The Z-Power** (`CueKind.ZPower`, 1.4 s, `BattleAnimator.ZPowerTime`), on the line "X surrounds itself with its Z-Power!", in the colour of the Z-Move's type (a status move's Z-Power in its own type's):
  - *The pose*: the Pokémon gathers itself (the charge pose of plan 25 · Y5) from the line until its Z-Move is let go.
  - *The frame*: a tall frame of light round the body (a rounded rectangle of ribbons, 1.25 *w* either side of its middle, from its feet to 1.2 *h*: a body of the type's colour at 0.7 with its light colour added over) rising out of the ground over its first 0.45 s and held, pulsing; motes of the type's light rising inside it; a ring at its feet.
  - *The mark*: a Z of three ribbons in the type's light colour over a body of its main colour, 0.36 *h* wide, over the head at 1.12 to 1.28 *h*, drawn stroke by stroke from 0.35 s to 0.65 s and held.
  - *The body*: a cast of the type's colour pulsing to 30 % through the frame.
  - Everything is gone by 1.4 s. Sound `z_power`.
- **A Z-Move** (`EffectCue.ZMove`, 1.5 s, `BattleAnimator.ZMoveTime`) lands at `ImpactTime` with its damage, as every move does, and goes on growing for a second after: a short gathering on the user (its type's glow and a ring, 0 to 0.3 s), the crossing (0.1 to 0.35 s), the landing bigger than any move's (the type's burst at 1.6, two rings), and then the type's own set piece round the target to 1.4 s, all faded by 1.5 s:
  | Type | Its Z-Move | The set piece |
  |---|---|---|
  | Normal | Breakneck Blitz | the user's speed lines, a great star of light and three rings in turn |
  | Fighting | All-Out Pummeling | twelve fists of light raining blows on the target from every side |
  | Flying | Supersonic Skystrike | a dive out of the sky in long streaks and a ring of wind on the ground |
  | Poison | Acid Downpour | a pool of poison under the target and a downpour of purple drops |
  | Ground | Tectonic Rage | the ground split under the target in cracks and rocks thrown up, a shake of 0.5 |
  | Rock | Continental Crush | a rain of great boulders onto the target |
  | Bug | Savage Spin-Out | silk threads wound round and round the target in a cocoon |
  | Ghost | Never-Ending Nightmare | a pool of shadow under the target and wisps and claws of shadow rising out of it |
  | Steel | Corkscrew Crash | a drill of steel streaks turning into the target, sparks thrown off |
  | Fire | Inferno Overdrive | a column of fire on the target, flames whirling up it |
  | Water | Hydro Vortex | a whirlpool of drops round the target, rising |
  | Grass | Bloom Doom | flowers of light opening round the target's feet and petals and leaves thrown up |
  | Electric | Gigavolt Havoc | bolts striking down onto the target from the sky, one after another |
  | Psychic | Shattered Psyche | rings of pink light closing in on the target and bursting outward |
  | Ice | Subzero Slammer | shards of ice closing on the target in a ring and frost thrown out |
  | Dragon | Devastating Drake | dragon fire streaming round the target in two spirals |
  | Dark | Black Hole Eclipse | a disc of dark behind the target (so its body stands before it), its rim of violet light drawing motes in |
