  | Fairy | Twinkle Tackle | a field of stars, hearts and sparkles round the target |

  A type's Z-Move is the same recipe whatever the move it was made of (a physical or a special one alike).
- **The species' own Z-Moves** are each built from the same pieces, in their type's colours unless said: *Catastropika*, bolts from the sky as Gigavolt Havoc and a ball of lightning dropping on the target; *Sinister Arrow Raid*, arrows of shadow raining down; *Malicious Moonsault*, a dark moon rising over the target and dropping on it; *Oceanic Operetta*, a great bubble of water rising over the target and breaking on it; *Guardian of Alola*, a giant palm of light pressing down on the target; *Soul-Stealing 7-Star Strike*, seven stars round the target, then a fist of shadow through them; *Stoked Sparksurfer*, a wave carrying bolts across; *Pulverizing Pancake*, a dark shadow growing on the ground under the target and a ring as it is flattened, a shake of 0.45; *Extreme Evoboost*, on the user, eight auras rising in turn in the colours of Eevee's evolutions; *Genesis Supernova*, a pink sphere of psychic light swelling on the target and bursting; *10,000,000 Volt Thunderbolt*, seven bolts of seven colours converging on the target; *Light That Burns the Sky*, a pillar of white-gold light falling on the target; *Searing Sunraze Smash*, a sun disc behind the user and a streak of gold into the target; *Menacing Moonraze Maelstrom*, a violet moon over the user and a beam of moonlight; *Let's Snuggle Forever*, a cloud of dust round the target with hearts and stars flying out; *Splintered Stormshards*, shards of rock spiralling up round the target; *Clangorous Soulblaze*, rings of sound pounding the target and gold glints.
- **With the scene off** (plan 08 · P1): none of these plays; the sound does, and a Pokémon's shape changes as the line appears.
- **Sounds** (*docs/sound-effects.md*, "Battles"): `mega_evolve`, `primal_reversion`, `ultra_burst`, `z_power` and `z_move_<type>`, one to a type (a species' own Z-Move is heard as its type's). They are set pieces of up to 2 s, longer than a move's `use_` sound (`SoundTests` allows them 2 s by name).

### Dynamax, Max Moves and Terastallization (plan 25 · Y15)

Plan 06 · R22's and R23's events (`Dynamaxed`, `DynamaxEnded`, `MaxMoveUsed` on a Max Move's own `MoveShown`, `Terastallized`) given looks of their own. Terastallization has no session of its own in this plan (R23 left its look to plan 25): it is done here, beside Dynamax. **Every look and beat is our own**, as Y14's are, and the same rules hold: pure functions of the cue's age and seed (`MoveFx.Giants.cs`, `MoveFx.MaxMoves.cs`), under `MoveFx.MaxQuads` (124) at every age, no screen flash over 0.35 or longer than a third of a second, no shake over 0.5, what circles a body split at its middle, nothing that stands over a Pokémon (a sign, an orb, a fist, a crown, a wreath) higher than 1.3 *h* above its feet, where only small things thrown out or falling out of the sky pass (*h* is the body as it stands, giant or not).

- **The Dynamax colours** are our own: red `232,40,84`, pink `255,120,170`, a cloud's dark red `90,24,48` and its light rim `255,176,200`. A Gigantamax adds gold `255,206,96` to them.
- **Dynamax** (`CueKind.Dynamax`, 1.6 s, `BattleAnimator.DynamaxTime`), on the line "X Dynamaxed!" (or "Gigantamaxed"):
  - *Gathering* (0 to 0.6 s): red clouds (`Smoke`, the dark red with the pink added over) gather round the feet, turning, drawing in from 1.6 *w* to 0.9 *w*; a column of red light rises up through the body (a ribbon from the feet to 1.25 *h*, pink core added over a red body).
  - *The body*: the shader's flash casts it red (`MoveFx.BodyCast`: `232,40,84`, up to 0.75 by 0.6 s, back to the lasting pulse by 1.3 s). **It grows** from 0.3 s to 0.9 s (eased out) to `BattleAnimator.DynamaxScaleFoe` of its size on the foe's platform (1.25: more, and a tall foe's head leaves the overview's top) and `DynamaxScaleOwn` on the player's own (1.3) (`BattleAnimator.GiantOf`). **In a battle of two or more a side it grows less**: `DynamaxScaleFoeDouble` 1.12 and `DynamaxScaleOwnDouble` 1.15 (`BattleAnimator.GiantScale`, chosen by `Paired(side)`; the user's ruling of 2026-10-31), so that at the overview a giant keeps clear of the partner beside it, as near as a wing tip (the test allows 2% of the gap between their places). Everything sized by the place's height follows the smaller size by itself (the wreath, the growth's clouds and column, a Max Move's aim, the giant shot's frame); the boss box and the HP boxes do not change. A Gigantamax takes its form 0.6 s in (`DynamaxSwapTime`), inside the red light, as a Mega Evolution does; a form with no model of its own shows its species'.
  - *The burst* (0.9 s): a red ring facing the lens out to 1.4 *h*, cloud puffs thrown out, a screen flash of pale pink 0.25 over 0.25 s; a Gigantamax throws gold sparks with them and three short bolts of gold play in its clouds.
  - *Settling* (to 1.6 s): the clouds settle round its feet into the wreath it keeps.
- **The giant's arrival** (plan 25 · YD1; `docs/plans/finished/Y-dynamax-punch.md`), laid over the Dynamax above, none of it with the Battle Scene off but what lasts (the sky's wash and its clouds are state of a giant standing):
  - *The trainer's call* (`EffectCue.Lead`, `BattleAnimator.DynamaxCallTime` 1.0 s, before the growth; the growth's own times above all move on by it): the trainer comes back to the edge of their platform (the foe's beside it where a speaker stands, `BattleStage.SpeakerSpot`; the player's on the west side of theirs, `BattleStage.CallerSpot`) over 0.3 s, turned toward their Pokémon. The Pokémon is drawn back into red light toward the trainer's hand (0.05 to 0.35 s, the recall's red beam from it to the hand), and the ball in the hand swells from a thrown ball's size to six times it (four on the player's own trainer, who is smaller in the picture) from 0.35 to 0.65 s, held over the hand by its own half, wrapped in a red-violet glow (`210,40,120`) with a red glow on the band at the wrist that flares as it swells. The arm is held back (the throw's wind-up) through the swell and the throw begins at 0.7 s, the ball leaving the hand at the throw's release and arcing over the place by 1.0 s, where it opens in a burst of red light out of which the Pokémon rises, grown back from 0.12 of its size to its own in 0.3 s (eased past) and on into the giant. The trainer walks off the way they came from 1.0 s, gone by 1.4. Only the **first Dynamax of a battle** has it, and only in a single battle, not at the Frontier, not behind a doll or out of sight: every other is the growth alone, as before (plan question 3).
  - *The lines wait for it* (plan 25 · YD6; `BattleEngine.GrowthHold`): a line read while a growth (its call included) still plays goes on only once the growth is over, the line read staying in the box meanwhile, as a thrown ball holds the lines; so the next line, the next turn and its menu never come while the Pokémon is still in the ball or growing. The growth's sound, cry and form keep their own beats through the hold, and nothing waits with the scene off.
  - *The Champion's words* (plan 25 · YD6; `TrainerLineSet.Giant`): a trainer with a line for it (each Champion's Mega Max team has one) says it once the growth is over, through the message box with their name on its pill, stepping back onto their platform as for any line of theirs (plan 11 · C12, C13); the trainers' lines option turns it off.
  - *The hit-stop at the burst*: the growth's looks are held 0.12 s at the burst (0.9 s after the growth begins) and caught up after, so the cue still ends on time (`Directions.Remap`, the shown age of plan 25 · Y17; `Directions.GiantStop` under `BattleCamera.Motion`, so nought with REDUCE MOTION). The body's growth and the animator's clock are untouched.
  - *The shockwave*: at the burst a second ring lies on the ground and runs out across the stage to 3 *h* (depth-tested, pink `255,120,170` going to nothing), and six puffs of dust (`200,180,170`) are thrown off the platform's rim along the ground, gone by 0.7 s after. About 8 quads more: the growth stays under 66.
  - *The cry*: at the burst the Pokémon's own cry plays in `CryMode.Giant` (a fifth down, doubled an octave below, in a long hall: echoes every 0.14 s at 0.55 for a third of a second more), the music dipping under it as under any cry. The Dynamax's sound waits for the call and stays on its beats; the call has its own (`dynamax_call`: the beam's draw, a swelling hum, a whoosh at the throw).
  - *The giant's sky* (`Graphics/GiantSky.cs`): while any Pokémon on the field stands giant the stage, its lights, its sky and its trainers are washed toward a red-violet `0.42,0.08,0.30` by 0.3 and greyed by 0.12 (the Pokémon never, as every stage light of Y16), eased in over the growth (from the call's end to the burst) and out over its end's 1.0 s; a Max Move's own light lays over it. Outdoors, four banks of red-violet cloud (`Smoke`, `150,40,90` at 0.45 of the wash) stand low on the sky north of the stage, turning slowly: at most `GiantSky.CloudCap` (8) quads.
- **While it lasts** (`CombatantView.Dynamaxed`, drawn by `MoveFx.Giants`, after the conditions): a wreath of six red clouds (`232,40,84` deepened by a third toward the cloud's dark red, at 0.7) turning slowly round its feet, *w* + 0.12 *h* out (the half behind it depth-tested), pink light on three, a red glow under it and two motes rising up its body; at most 12 quads a Pokémon. Round the feet and never over the head: a giant's head stands near the picture's top, and over the player's own it would stand under the foe's HP box. The body pulses red (`MoveFx.GiantCast`: 0.12 to 0.24 at 1.2 Hz), under any cue's own cast. A Gigantamax's wreath has gold glints in it.
- **Its end, the fall** (`CueKind.DynamaxEnd`, 1.0 s; plan 25 · YD3), on the third turn's line: *the shudder* (0 to 0.3 s): the body shaken where it stands (a lurch toward and away of 0.03 of its height at 14 Hz, dying away; `Directions.Shudder`), red light bleeding out of it in eight streams rising off its body, the wreath's clouds drawn in to it and up; *the collapse* (0.3 to 0.75 s): it falls in on itself to 0.95 of its own size (`BattleAnimator.DynamaxFallStart`, `DynamaxShrunk`), the form (a Gigantamax's own) going back half way (0.5 s, `DynamaxEndSwapTime`), and the clouds are thrown out off the platform as six puffs of dust with a pink ring on the ground out to 1.6 *h*; *the overshoot* (0.75 to 0.9 s): back up to its own size. The giant shot holds it to 0.7 s. `giant_fall` sounds at the collapse after `dynamax_end`'s sigh. **On a faint while giant**: the faint waits 0.45 s more (`BattleAnimator.GiantFallTime`) while the body sinks a quarter of its height into its clouds and shrinks by the same curve, `giant_fall` at once, the giant shot on it until the faint's own; a recall shrinks it as it goes with nothing played.
- **The three turns seen** (plan 25 · YD3): the wreath thins a cloud a turn (six clouds with three turns left, five with two, four on the last) and the body's pulse quickens (1.2, 1.6, 2.2 Hz); on the last turn the clouds flicker (their strength 0.6 to 1 at 9 Hz) and a heartbeat bed (`AmbienceBed.Giant`) is heard under the music until it ends. The pill on the HP box ticks as a menu settles a lower count (0.4 s, `CombatantView.TickAge`): the old number falls 14 units and fades, the new drops in from 14 above; never up, and with the scene off as well, since it is state.
- **A Max Move** (`EffectCue.MaxMove`, 1.5 s, `BattleAnimator.MaxMoveTime`) lands at `ImpactTime` with its damage: a red pulse round the user (0 to 0.3 s), the crossing (a beam of its type's colour sheathed in Dynamax red for a special move, the speed lines and a red streak for a physical one, 0.1 to 0.35 s), the landing (its type's burst at 1.5, two rings, one of them red), then a red glow behind the target and the type's set piece to 1.4 s, all faded by 1.5 s. One recipe to a type, whatever move it was made of:
  | Type | Its Max Move | The set piece |
  |---|---|---|
  | Normal | Max Strike | five stars of light falling round the target and bursting where they land |
  | Fighting | Max Knuckle | a great fist of light falling on the target out of the sky, a ring where it lands |
  | Flying | Max Airstream | a tornado: rings of wind rising round the target, wider as they rise |
  | Poison | Max Ooze | three spouts of ooze bursting up round the target, bubbles rising |
  | Ground | Max Quake | the ground heaving in a ring of slabs thrown up round the target, a shake of 0.45 |
  | Rock | Max Rockfall | pillars of stone bursting up out of the ground round the target |
  | Bug | Max Flutterby | a swarm of glowing motes spiralling in on the target |
  | Ghost | Max Phantasm | wisps of shadow closing in on the target into a dark orb that bursts |
  | Steel | Max Steelspike | spikes of steel thrust up out of the ground round the target in a ring |
  | Fire | Max Flare | a great ball of fire falling on the target and bursting in flames |
  | Water | Max Geyser | three geysers bursting up round the target, drops falling back |
  | Grass | Max Overgrowth | leaves bursting up out of the ground and whirling up round the target |
  | Electric | Max Lightning | a ring of bolts standing up out of the ground round the target at once |
  | Psychic | Max Mindstorm | a sphere of pink light closing round the target and ripples out of it |
  | Ice | Max Hailstorm | hail falling on the target and frost thrown up |
  | Dragon | Max Wyrmwind | a column of dragon fire coiling up round the target |
  | Dark | Max Darkness | spikes of shadow rising round the target and a dark orb over it |
  | Fairy | Max Starfall | stars falling on the target in a shower, a ring of pink light |
- **Max Guard** (on the user): a dome of red hexagons (`Hex`, pink edges added over the red, 14 of them) closing round the body from 0.05 s and held to 1.1 s, a ring at its feet; a glint where a blow would land. **A blow on it** (plan 25 · YD3; `EffectCue.OnGuard`): a move that comes to nothing on a Pokémon whose Max Guard has gone up this turn (`CombatantView.MaxGuarded`, set by Max Guard's own cue and cleared when a menu settles) stops at the dome's surface (its crossing ends *w* + 0.2 *h* short of the body's middle, toward the user), and at the impact the dome is struck: its fourteen hexagons stand again round the body at their held places (0.45 red, from the impact to 0.6 s after), the half that faces the blow lit pink by how squarely it faces it, ten sparks of white and pink thrown from where the blow ended and a pink ring there; `max_guard_hit`, a hard ring, is heard as it lands (with the scene off too). Never for a miss, nor for a blow on a place with no Max Guard up. 26 quads over the move's own.
- **The Max Move finisher** (plan 25 · YD2; `docs/plans/finished/Y-dynamax-punch.md`), laid over every landed Max Move and G-Max Move (not Max Guard), none of it with the Battle Scene off but its sounds:
  - *The hit-stop*: the cue's looks, the target's body and the camera are held 0.12 s at the impact (`Directions.MaxStop`, under `BattleCamera.Motion`) and caught up after, so the cue still ends at 1.5 s; the line, the damage and the bar keep the clock.
  - *The shatter* (`MoveFx.MaxShatter`): at the impact twelve pieces of the type's own shape (flames, leaves, stones, drops, sparks, shards, stars, motes, wisps, bubbles, fists, feathers, rings) in its main and light colours are thrown out of the target's middle on arcs, 1.3 *h* out and falling, gone by 0.75 s after; a second ring of the type's light lies on the ground out to 2.4 *h* 0.08 s after the first. 13 quads.
  - *The shake*: 0.5 (the cap) for a physical Max Move, 0.35 for a special one, rising over 0.04 s at the impact and gone by 0.5 s after (Max Quake keeps its own 0.45 where it is the stronger).
  - *The target thrown* (`Directions.MaxThrown`): back from the user 0.25 of its height and pressed to 0.85 of it from the impact on (at once, so the hit-stop holds it there), then staggering home over 0.5 s (a lurch forward of a fifth of the throw as it settles); a G-Max Move whose mark falls presses it down instead (sunk 0.06, pressed 0.18), one whose mark rises tosses it up 0.15. In the body's own height, so a giant is thrown no further off its platform than anyone; exactly still before the impact and after.
  - *The box punched* (`BattleAnimator.Punch`, `ModernUi.PunchOf`): as it lands the target's HP box jolts (6 units across and 3 down, shaking at 11 Hz and gone by 0.3 s; none under `BattleCamera.Motion` nought), the share it loses stays white on the bar for 0.3 s (the bar's own `DisplayedHp` as the blow lands, never the rules') and drains after at the bar's own pace, and the HP number of the player's box swells by a quarter and back over 0.25 s.
- **A G-Max Move** is its type's Max Move with a mark of its own added, chosen from six kinds of motion (shapes circling the target at mid height, falling round it, rising in a column, thrown out of it, lying round its feet, or one great sign over it at 1.15 *h*), in the species' own colours: *Befuddle*, scales of violet and gold circling; *Cannonade*, orbs of water falling; *Centiferno*, flames coiling round; *Chi Strike*, gold stars thrown out; *Cuddle*, hearts rising; *Depletion*, plates of steel blue lying round; *Drum Solo*, notes thrown out; *Finale*, cream motes rising; *Fireball*, a great fireball over it; *Foam Burst*, bubbles thrown out; *Gold Rush*, gold rings falling; *Gravitas*, violet rings lying round; *Hydrosnipe*, a target over it; *Malodor*, green smoke rising; *Meltdown*, drops of steel falling; *One Blow*, a dark fist over it; *Rapid Flow*, waves circling; *Replenish*, berries thrown out; *Resonance*, pale green waves rising; *Sandblast*, sand circling; *Smite*, pink stars falling; *Snooze*, Z's rising; *Steelsurge*, copper spikes lying round; *Stonesurge*, stones thrown out; *Stun Shock*, sparks circling; *Sweetness*, petals thrown out; *Tartness*, leaves circling; *Terror*, an eye over it; *Vine Lash*, leaves lying round; *Volcalith*, embers rising; *Volt Crash*, crosses of light falling; *Wildfire*, flames lying round; *Wind Rage*, feathers circling. (Ten of these have since a set piece of their own in place of their type's and their mark: below.)
- **The Gigantamax signatures** (plan 25 · YD7, parts a and b; `MoveFx.GMaxOwn.cs`; our own): ten G-Max Moves lay a set piece of their own over the Max Moves' shared beats (the pulse, the crossing, the landing, the finisher's shatter, shake and red glow) in place of their type's piece and their mark, faded by 1.4 s with the set piece's envelope, each at most 124 quads with those beats:
  | G-Max Move | Its set piece |
  |---|---|
  | Wildfire (Charizard) | ten walls of flame (orange `255,120,40` and deep red `200,50,30` under gold `255,210,90`) springing up one after another in a ring round the target's feet, 0.025 s apart, roaring to 0.85 *h* and flickering; embers lifting off them; the ground inside glowing deep red under an orange ring; as they die down from 0.8 s, six flames lying burning round its feet |
  | Volt Crash (Pikachu) | a great forked bolt out of the sky (yellow `255,230,60`, core `255,255,210`) striking the target's head at the impact with a pale flash of 0.22 for 0.24 s; six bolts running out from its feet along the ground, one after another, crackling for 0.55 s; a disc of yellow light and a turning ring under it; crosses of light falling round it |
  | Terror (Gengar) | a pool of shadow (`36,18,54`) spreading under it with a violet ring; six hands of shadow rising out of the pool round it from 0.45 s, each with a violet glow behind, leaning in and closing on it from 0.85 s; six wisps of violet and lilac circling; a great lilac eye with a red pupil opening over it at 1.08 *h*, held and closing by 1.35 s |
  | Resonance (Lapras) | three curtains of aurora (pale green `150,240,190`, sea-blue `150,210,255`, violet `190,160,255`) hanging over the stage behind the target, each **eight upright folds** (soft glows stretched three times as tall as wide, matter at 0.7 to 1, each swaying and brightening on its own beat) along a waving line from over the target out to the sides; they open out from the middle over 0.4 s. A curtain is upright folds and nothing along its length: one wide ribbon along the line read as a streak across the trees (part a's first look), and so did a thin seam along its foot. Four rings of sound rising round it; six snowflakes turning round it; motes of their light falling from the curtains |
  | Replenish (Snorlax) | fourteen berries (red, violet, blue, gold, pink: our own) thrown up out of it on arcs, landing round it with a small bounce and a green puff; leaves drifting down; from 0.75 s on the user's own side a green glow at its feet and five berries popping up round them |
  | Chi Strike (Machamp; part b) | four great fists of gold light (`255,210,80`, a pale core `255,250,220`) driving in on the target from four sides at mid height one after another, 0.07 s apart, each with a short trail of light behind it and a white-gold impact where it lands; at the fourth a burst of ten gold sparks and an upright ring of gold spreading round its middle; then five gold stars circling over its head at 1.0 to 1.15 *h* (its blows now strike true) |
  | Gold Rush (Meowth; part b) | a shower of eighteen gold coins (`255,200,60`, rim `200,140,30`) falling out of the sky round the target, each spinning (narrowing to its edge and back), landing close before its feet (on the camera's side, where the body hides none) in a heap, the later coins nearer and higher on the pile (up to 0.12 *h*), each lying tipped a little on a darker rim, 0.13 *h* across; glints (white-gold stars) winking on the heap; a gold glow on the ground under it |
  | Cuddle (Eevee; part b) | twelve pink hearts (`255,150,190`, cream `240,214,170`) floating in from round the stage and gathering into a ring hugging the target at its middle, the ring squeezing twice; then a great soft heart of pink light blooming over its body, sparkles round it, and four little hearts popping up over its head as the hug lets go |
  | Befuddle (Butterfree; part b) | a pair of great wings of violet light (`176,120,230`, edged gold `255,214,90`) opening behind the target and beating twice, each side a butterfly's two lobes (a forewing 0.52 *h* slanting up and out, a smaller, darker hindwing 0.32 *h* below it), so they read as wings and not as two discs; a falling spiral of twenty-four glittering scales round it in the three colours of what it may bring (poison violet `190,90,220`, paralysis yellow `255,220,70`, sleep pale blue `170,210,255`); and, as the scales settle, three signs popping over its head one after another (a bubble, a spark, a Z) |
  | Depletion (Duraludon; part b) | a tower of hex panels of steel-blue light (`90,130,190`, lit cyan `160,230,255`) standing behind the target, two columns of six, every panel lit at the impact and going dark one by one from the top down, as a building's windows go out; ten motes of cyan drawn up out of the target and fading as they rise; eight hex plates lying round its feet, lit cyan and dimming to dark steel |
  A signature is heard by a sound of its own (`gmax_<name>`, `SoundBank.GMaxSound`), every other G-Max Move by its type's.
- **A Gigantamax's own colours** (plan 25 · YD7, part a): a Gigantamax's growth, its wreath and its end take its G-Max Move's two colours (the mark's, above) in place of the Dynamax's red in their clouds: each cloud by turns in one of the two deepened a third toward the cloud's dark `90,24,48` as the red is, the clouds' lights in the brighter of the two a fifth toward white (the other by turns where it reads), the burst's thrown cloud in both; a colour too dark to read in a cloud (Terror's, One Blow's) lifted halfway to the other. The column of light, the rings, the flash, the body's cast and pulse and the gold sparks and bolts stay the Dynamax's, so a Gigantamax still reads as a Dynamax first. Which G-Max Move is the form's is read from the moves' `gigantamaxOf` (`BattleAnimator.GMaxOfForm`: the form's name less "-Gmax", or the shorter names it is made of), carried on the cue (`EffectCue.GMax`) and the place (`CombatantView.GMax`), settled at a menu from the rules' own form.
- **Max Moves on the field** (plan 25 · YD4; `docs/plans/finished/Y-dynamax-punch.md`): a weather, a terrain or a side's condition told on the lines of a landed Max Move (the last move the screen showed, before a menu opens) comes out from where its blow landed; what lasts afterwards is drawn exactly as it would have been, and none of it plays with the Battle Scene off. Over `BattleAnimator.BlowArrivalTime` (1.1 s, longer than a look's own coming in, so the finisher's pull-back to 1.4 s sees it run out):
  - *A weather* (`EffectCue.FromBlow`, `ArenaFx.WeatherArrival`): a ring of the weather's colour on the ground running out from the struck Pokémon's feet to 14 units, a fainter one 0.1 s behind it, and six of the weather's own pieces (sparks of sun, drops of rain, puffs of sand, hailstones, flakes, wisps) thrown out level from its body; the cue's shower leaves out as many streaks as the arrival draws, so the cue keeps its 124.
  - *A terrain or a G-Max side's ring* (`FieldLook.Tell(event, from)`, `ConditionView.From`, `BattleFieldFx.Arrival`): a flood of its colour lying on the ground from the struck Pokémon's feet out to the far rim of where it settles (both platforms for a terrain), its front a band of two rings close together, a glow at the feet as it starts; matter, alpha-blended, at most 0.75 and 1 of alpha. 4 quads (`BattleFieldFx.ArrivalCap`).
  - *G-Max Steelsurge*: its five splinters are thrown from the impact on arcs (half the platform's radius high) to where they hover, spinning, whole as they fly, and stand where they would have stood once landed. In flight each is drawn apart from the set piece's own grey shards: 1.7 times its size at the impact easing to its own as it lands, in a cold blue-white (222, 236, 255) on a dark steel rim (30, 40, 62, 1.3 times as big), with a cyan trail (120, 214, 255) stretched behind it along its way; the trail and the rim take the place of its shadow and glint, so it draws the same three quads in the air as at rest.
- **A Mega Dynamax** (plan 25 · YD4; R20c's agreed look), told by its form's kind (`FormKind.MegaDynamax`; `EffectCue.MegaMax`, `CombatantView.MegaMax`): the Dynamax's red is turned to the Mega's four colours, violet `186,96,255`, pink `255,120,200`, orange `255,176,70`, cyan `110,220,255`. The growth's gathering clouds take them by turns (each deepened 0.35 toward the cloud's dark red, so it reads over any ground), its column is violet under pink, its rings violet and cyan, its burst's puffs violet going to orange, its flash pale violet `236,214,255`; *the Mega mark* (a white ring round a four-pointed star with a glow of each colour on it, 0.16 *h*, swelling a quarter at the burst) flares on the body's middle on the camera's side from 0.3 s, brightest at the burst and gone by 1.45 s. Its wreath's clouds and glows are the four colours by turns, the glow under it and the body's cast and pulse the Dynamax's red turned halfway to violet; its end's streams go through the four. A Gigantamax is never a Mega Dynamax. The trainer's call lights a violet four-pointed star (the Key Stone) beside the band's red at the wrist.
- **Terastallization** (`CueKind.Tera`, 1.7 s, `BattleAnimator.TeraTime`), on the line "X Terastallized into the T type!", in the Tera Type's colours (`MoveFx.ColorsOf`); a **Stellar** one in seven colours turning (red, orange, gold, green, cyan, blue, violet, `MoveFx.StellarColour`):
  - *The shell* (0.1 to 0.85 s): facets of crystal (`Hex` with a `Shard` glint, the type's light colour at 0.75 over its main) fly in from 1.8 *w* and lock round the body on three rings (at 0.25, 0.55 and 0.85 *h*, eight, seven and five facets), the half behind it depth-tested; the body takes the type's light colour through the flash (to 0.8 by 0.8 s).
  - *Shattering* (0.9 s, `TeraSwapTime`): the facets are thrown out and spin away, a ring of the type's light out to 1.4 *h*, a screen flash of the type's light colour 0.25. A Terapagos takes its Stellar Form here.
  - *The crown* (from 0.9 s): a crown of crystal just over the head at 1.03 *h* (five upright facets in a fan, 0.32 *h* across, the middle one tallest, a glint over it) rises into place and stays.
- **While it lasts**: the crown (five facets and one glint, 6 quads) over the head, and a sheen of the type's light colour on the body (`MoveFx.GiantCast`: 0.1 to 0.2, slowly, a Stellar one's colour turning), until the battle ends. Behind a doll, out of sight, fainting, recalled or caught it is not drawn, as a condition that lasts isn't (`BattleAnimator.LastingShown`).
- **With the scene off** (plan 08 · P1): none of these plays and nothing grows; the sounds do, the forms change as the lines appear, and the HP boxes still say what they say below.
- **Sounds** (*docs/sound-effects.md*, "Battles"): `dynamax`, `gigantamax`, `dynamax_end`, `max_guard`, `max_move_<type>` (one to a type; a G-Max Move is heard as its type's but for the signatures, which have `gmax_<name>`), `terastallize` and `tera_stellar`. Set pieces of up to 2 s, by name (`SoundBank.IsSetPiece`).

### Light, shadow and grading (day)

| Setting | Value |
|---|---|
| Sun | normalize(−0.5, 0.9, 0.5), colour 0.47, 0.43, 0.35 |
| Sky / ground ambient | 0.54, 0.60, 0.72 / 0.50, 0.50, 0.40 |
| Shadows and cloud shade | as in the field; cloud shade 16 % |
| Fog | aerial perspective toward the sky's horizon colour, 35 % at the hills (from 40 to 140 units) |
| Ambient occlusion | radius 0.6 units, strength 0.6: contact shade under Pokémon, tufts and tree crowns |
| Stage outlines | drawn where depth steps, on the near side only, 1 px wide, darkening the surface by up to 35 % (inverse depth is flat across the ground, so only silhouettes and creases fire) |
| Tilt-shift | radius 2 outside ±50 %; no wide blur |
| Bloom | threshold 0.86, strength 0.28 |
| Grade | saturation 1.05, contrast 1.05, shadows × (0.94, 0.97, 1.06), highlights × (1.04, 1.01, 0.95), vignette 0.10 |

## Open world (plan 32)

What the open world's renderer draws (plan 32 · OW8 and OW9, `Graphics/OpenWorld/`): the ground, the water, the sky, the light, the weather in the air, the camera and the shadows of an open map (Galar's and Paldea's outdoors and continuous caves), and since OW9 its buildings, trees, grass, people, Pokémon and the land beyond the region to the horizon (below the covers' table). The gate's prototype (the harness's `openproto`, nothing of it in the game) keeps its own path and OW0's numbers, which the user passed on 2026-11-07 and which these grow from ("The prototype", at the end).

| Setting | Value |
|---|---|
| Units | one world unit is a metre (the grid's is a tile); x east, y up, +z south, as the field's |
| Scene | 1920×1080 on every preset: a step of half of High's 4K target, two thirds of Medium's, the whole of Low's (`OpenRenderer.Step`); dynamic resolution (plan 16 · T23) steps under it, never over it; the interface at 4K over it, as everywhere |
| Camera | orbit behind the player (`OpenWorld/OrbitCamera.cs`): 5.5 m back, pitched 16° down at a point 1.35 m over the feet, vertical lens 50°, near plane 0.3 m and far 1,400 m; no straightening of upright things |
| The camera's arm | a ray from the point back to the camera against the collision mesh, stopped 0.3 m short of what it meets and never under 0.6 m: held in at once, let out again at 3 m a second, so walking out of a tunnel the camera swings back out instead of jumping |
| Turning | the shoulders (Q and E, the pad's L and R) turn it 100° a second; it eases behind a walking player (half the way in 0.9 s), once two seconds have passed since it was last turned by hand, and never while the player walks toward it (within 45° of straight on) |
| A script's look | `camera look <marker>`: over the line's seconds (smoothstep) the point the camera looks at goes from over the player to 1 m over the marker and the camera goes round behind the player, away from it; it holds there while the script runs, and comes home over 0.8 s once the script has ended |
| Ground | each texel of 16 a metre takes one cover (below) and its tile's colour there: the cover of the triangle's corner whose weight is greatest, where the weights are the corners' barycentric weights at the texel's middle (corners of one cover summed first) jittered by ±0.28 per texel, so the border between two covers is a ragged pixel edge two or three texels wide and never a blend; whole texels near (nearest), blended between mip levels far |
| Projection | a texel's tile is laid by the face's own axis (top, east-west or north-south), chosen per 1/16 m cube by the normal's weights to the fourth power with a jitter of its own: no tile is stretched down a cliff, a cave's wall or its roof, and where two projections meet they are dithered, never blended |
| Light on the ground | the battle scenery's (`worldRamp` 0.85: a soft two-tone band), its sky and ground ambient by the normal, the sun with the three cascades' shadows and the cloud shade, fog by distance |
| Levels | the block of three chunks by three round the player's at level 0 (0.5 m), then level 1 (level 0 thinned to 6 cm) to 300 m, and level 2 (30 cm) beyond; the player's chunk moves on only 4 m past its border, and a chunk keeps the far level it has within 10 m either side of 300 m, so walking along a border doesn't swap levels back and forth (`OpenWorld/OrbitCamera.cs`, `OpenRings`). A chunk is drawn at the level it has until the one wanted is loaded (never a hole); uploads take at most 2.5 ms a frame but where a chunk the camera sees has nothing yet. Level 0's apron is drawn with it and casts. Its skirts are not drawn: hung from a roof along a chunk's border (the tunnel's portal) they stood in the air as fins, and the apron and the thinned levels' border rows, which are level 0's own vertices, close every seam without them |
| Beyond the region | the rings to the horizon (OW9, `OpenHorizon`; "Beyond the region", below): OW8's band, a ring round the region's extent that begins a metre inside its border, 4 cm under the border's own ground there (the lower of the ground 0.3 m and 1 m inside, read every 2 m round the border from the ground the rules collide with), keeps that height 4 m out and eases to the border's median height by 124 m through rings at 12, 28 and 60 m (smoothstep), lit and covered as level grass; and past it far hills and their forests out to 2.6 km, ending in the fog. The land never ends against the sky, no sliver of sky shows between the region's last ground and the land beyond, a river's channel cut by the border fades out within the ease instead of running to the horizon as a crease the outlines draw, and 4 cm is too little for the outlines to draw more than a hairline along the seam |

The covers' tiles: 32 texels square, repeating every 2 m (`OpenCoverArt`), each a flat base with clean-edged patches one step lighter and placed marks of two texels or more, as the field's grounds are ("Ground"). The base colours are the gate's, which the user passed.

| Cover | Base / patches | Marks |
|---|---|---|
| Grass | `104,190,98` / `120,200,102` | tufts `70,150,82` with a tip `160,222,122`, a "v" of three blades, two to a tile's quarter |
| Grass, light | `120,200,102` / `132,208,110` | tufts `88,168,90`, tip `170,228,130` |
| Grass, deep (slopes over 25°) | `80,162,88` / `92,174,94` | tufts `58,126,82`, tip `132,204,112` |
| Path | `222,204,160` / `232,218,180` | pebbles: 2×2 `168,160,150` with a light texel `208,202,192` and a shadow `200,178,128` under them |
| Rock | `150,140,128` / `164,154,142` | strata: bent lines `112,104,98` five to nine texels long, each with a light chip `196,188,176` over it |
| Rock, dark (over 63°) | `112,104,98` / `124,116,108` | strata `84,78,76`, chips `150,140,128` |
| Cave floor | `128,108,88` / `140,120,98` | cracks `96,80,66`, pebbles `164,150,132` |
| Highland (over 20 m) | `136,168,112` / `148,178,122` | tufts `104,138,86`, tip `178,204,148` |
| Sand | `238,224,172` / `246,236,196` | ripple arcs `214,196,140` |
| Snow | `186,204,232` / `196,212,238` | drift lines `168,188,224`, three texels long |
| Mud | `124,106,86` / `140,122,98` | wet patches `98,112,108`, five texels wide with a light texel |
| Climbable rock (plan 30 · FM12: the mount climbs it, plan 34 · PD2; laid only by paint) | `138,124,108` / `150,136,118` | ledges: level lines `96,86,78` four to seven texels long with a lip `188,176,158` over each, and holds of 2×2 `96,86,78` with a light texel, so a climbable cliff reads as warmer, ledged rock beside the strata of plain rock |

**Water** (`OpenTerrainMesh.WaterOf`, the water program of `OpenShaders`): the field water's look at 16 texels a metre, banded by the depth over the ground under it where the field's bands go by the distance from the shore. Shallow `116,184,232` under 0.3 m, mid `76,146,214` under 1.0 m, deep `58,118,190` beyond, the band edges breathing by a texel; foam `236,246,255` where it is shallower than 6 cm, lapping in and out, and a broken line `176,218,248` round 0.15 m; wave marks (light dashes `150,206,246` 4 to 8 texels long over a dark line `46,104,186`, drifting east two texels a second) never where it is shallower than 0.5 m; sparkles in sunlight. Each texel takes the depth at its middle, so the bands are whole texels. The surface over each 2 m cell is the bake's, its corners shared with the cells beside it (a river's slope has no steps); level 0's water is laid at 0.5 m with its depth read from level 0's ground, the far levels' at 2 m from their own; a cell beside the water is laid too, and what is dry of it is never drawn, so the shore is where the ground meets the water. The depth under a point is over the highest ground at or under the water there (the bed under a rock bridge, not the bridge) or, on a bank, over the lowest ground above it, so it is less than nought on the bank and comes to nought where the bank leaves the water: the shallows and the foam lie along every shore, and the deep band never meets the land. After dark the water is drawn darker and greyer, as the field's is. **Reflection**: on High and Medium, what stands over the water (the ground and the player) is mirrored about the water's height nearest the middle of the picture into the 1920×1080 mirror (plan 09 · L10), read once per texel in the mid and deep bands only, broken into runs and darkened toward its band as the field's; water more than 0.25 m off that height shows its bands alone, and from 0.1 m off it the mirrored texels thin out by a dither, so a sloping river shows no line across it where its mirror ends. Low mirrors nothing.

**The sky** (the sky program of `OpenShaders`): the battle's sky of the hour (`ArtLook.BattleRig`'s zenith, middle and horizon) by the angle over the horizon, a full-screen pass that follows the camera's pitch: the horizon's colour at the horizon, the middle by 10°, the zenith from 50° up; under the horizon the horizon's colour, which is the fog's. Clouds are a layer 600 m up: value noise of three octaves, soft-edged (from 0.52 to 0.70 of it), lit in the rig's cloud tint and shaded 22 % toward the sky's middle underneath, drifting east at 4 m a second and thinning to nothing within 4° of the horizon. After dark, stars: one cell in 60 of a grid of 0.5° holds one, a speck 0.12° across (two or three texels of the 1080p scene), twinkling, fading in from 1° to 3° over the horizon. Under the weather the sky is drawn toward the weather's grey, as a battle's is (`ArtLook.InBattleWeather`), a cloudy sky half as far as a fog's; in fog, a sandstorm, heavy snow or a blizzard, which close the view in, it is drawn three quarters of the way to the fog's own colour and the outlines at 30 %, so neither the sky's blue nor the ink of what the fog hides shows through.

**The light** (`OpenLook`): the battle's rig of the hour (`ArtLook.BattleRig`) with its fog drawn out to the horizon, from 110 m to 760 m and 78 % at the far edge, as the gate's; no tilt-shift and no wide blur (a camera low behind the player is no diorama), the battle's outlines, occlusion, bloom and grade; and the field's lit glass (OW9): its lamps and shops (`LampGlow`) and its homes' windows (`HomeGlow`) are the field rig's of the hour (`ArtLook.FieldRig`), since a battle's rig lights no glass, so the buildings glow after dark as a town's do ("Time of day"). **Weather** is the field's (`ArtLook.Weathered`: the sun lost, half of it scattered from the whole sky, the fog thicker and, in fog, a sandstorm or a blizzard, near), with the sky toward its grey as above.

**Under rock** (`OpenUnderRock`, in `OpenWorld/OrbitCamera.cs`; `OpenLook.UnderRock`): where a ray straight up from the camera meets the land within 30 m, the cave's air eases in over 0.8 s (smoothstep), and out again as fast: the cave rig's haze (`ArtLook.CaveRig`: dark, from 18 m to 75 m), its grade and strong vignette, and its light from the rock all round, lifted and warmed for caverns larger than a battle's stage (`0.34,0.35,0.42` from above, `0.32,0.28,0.25` from below). The sun stays the sky's: what it reaches through a mouth stays lit, and the rock's own shadows keep it from the rest. A warm light `1.0,0.78,0.50` at 1.1 hangs half a metre over the player's head, lighting the rock round them and falling to nothing at 18 m (its square), and the player is lit by six tenths of it, so a tunnel and a cavern read as rock lit round whoever stands in them and darkening into haze, never as grey. Nothing of the weather falls under rock.

**A place's own light** (plan 33 · GL6; `OpenLook.InMood`, eased by `OpenMoodEase`): an area of `world.json` may name a light of its own (`light`), held only while a flag is set where it names one (`lightWhile`), laid over the rig of the hour before the weather and the cave's air. It eases in and out over **1 s** of the frames' time (smoothstep), one light out before another comes in, and is there at once where the view is first placed (a warp, a load). **`night`** is the rig of 23:00 whatever the hour (Spikemuth's: a town of neon and shutters is never seen by day); **`darkestDay`** is the rig of 19:30 with the sun dulled to `1.05,0.55,0.55` of itself, the ambient from above `1.0,0.62,0.70` and from below `1.0,0.70,0.72` of itself, the fog `0.52,0.16,0.20` at 85 % at least, no cloud shade, and a sky of its own (zenith `58,14,30`, middle `132,34,52`, horizon `196,74,66`, cloud `120,40,60`, no stars): the Dynamax energy's red over Wyndon while `FLAG_GALAR_DARKEST_DAY` is set. With no light, or none of it eased in, the rig is the hour's to the bit.

**Weather in the air** (`OpenWeatherFx`): pieces in a box round the camera, 24 m across and 16 m tall, each moving in the world and coming round again on the box's far side, so nothing slides with the camera, none nearer the camera than 1.8 m (one streak would fill the picture), and none under the first ground the sky meets over its place (nothing falls in a tunnel seen from its mouth: the height of that ground is kept for each metre's column of the map, found once by a ray from far over it). Rain: 2,400 streaks 0.5 m long and 1.2 cm wide falling 9 m a second a little to the east, `196,214,236` at half strength (heavy rain and a storm 4,400); snow: 1,600 flakes 5 cm across falling 1.1 m a second with a sway of 0.3 m (heavy snow 3,000, a blizzard 4,000 at 4 m a second slanting hard); hail: 1,400 pellets 3 cm across at 12 m a second; a sandstorm: 2,600 streaks blown east at 8 m a second in `214,190,140`; ash: 900 flakes falling half a metre a second. Every piece is lit by the scene's light as it stands (so rain never glows at night), depth-tested and never writing depth; none under rock (the cave's share takes them away); a storm's lightning whitens the picture as the field's does (`WeatherFx.Lightning`).

**Shadows** (`OpenCascades`): three maps from the sun, each fitted round its slice of the view (the sphere round the slice) and snapped to its own texels so edges stay still as the camera moves: the first, twice the preset's shadow map across (4,096 on High and Medium, 2,048 on Low), for the view to 18 m (a sphere about 34 m across); the second, the preset's, to 60 m; the third to 220 m; past that nothing casts. Each takes what casts into it from 200 m toward the sun past its sphere (the hill over a cavern). The last 15 % of each slice blends into the next. The preset's taps (9, 4, 1). The ground (at the level it is drawn at, with level 0's apron) and the player cast. The player is lit by the first, which is bound where the field's one map is.

**The player** is plan 30 · FM4's grown kit (`PLAYER~Teen`), drawn at the capsule's feet between the field's last two ticks (`OpenClock.Between`), facing its yaw, walking and running by its speed.
**The mount** (plan 30 · FM13, `Graphics/OpenWorld/MountRig.cs`; Paldea's, plan 34): the ridden Pokémon is its **battle model** in the form `MountRules.FormFor` names, never a stand-in. Ridden it is its running form (Koraidon's Sprinting Build, Miraidon's Drive Mode) standing, trotting, sprinting and clinging to a face, its swimming form swimming and its gliding form gliding; a jump or a fall keeps the running form. Its limited form (Koraidon's Limited Build, Miraidon's Low-Power Mode) is only for getting on and off, as Scarlet and Violet keep it: nobody sits an upright biped.

| Setting | Value |
|---|---|
| Size | one for the whole species, so a change of form never jumps: Koraidon 2.3 m a model unit, Miraidon 2.4, which put the riding forms' backs about 1.15 m off the ground under a grown teen of 1.6 m. Not the forms' heights in metres (3.5 m and 2.8 m), which would sit the rider over two metres up |
| The seat | each form's seat line along its length (model units, +z ahead): a riding form's just behind the middle of its back (−0.03), the limited forms' behind the shoulders (Koraidon −0.13, Miraidon −0.12), Miraidon's Glide Mode the same, under its sail (nothing over 0.56 is its seat). The seat is the top of the body bone's own surface on that line (within 0.035 of the middle and 0.03 of the line, so a wing, a tail or a plume over the back is never sat on, nor Miraidon's sail), found once on the form's own mesh, and carried through the clips by the body's bone, so the rider rises and falls with its back. Its half width there is the body's widest within 0.14 under the seat |
| The rider | astride (style guide, "Grown animation", the rides), the root under the seat by the saddle (the hip joints' height less 0.075 of the body's size, as a sitter's) along the mount's own way up and turned as the mount is, so a rider climbs a face lying along its back. The stance by what the mount does: seated at the trot (its rock the mount's own stride), a dash over 8 m/s, swim (kneeling, the root raised by its saddle), glide, climb; the crouch eases in from 2 m/s to 8 |
| Its clips | its battle model's walk by distance over a stride: 2.6 m at the trot and the sprint, 2.2 m swimming, 1.4 m up a face; still in the air; eased in and out at 6 a second |
| How it stands | level at the feet, turned by the body's yaw; **gliding** 6° nose down; **swimming** afloat, dropped so its water line (0.13 model units under its seat) is the water's surface, the rider's knees clear of the water; **climbing** its belly to the face and its nose 75° up it, its back out of the face, standing 0.23 m (the capsule's radius less 0.12) off the capsule's axis toward the face |
| A change of form | 0.3 s: squashed by up to 12 % and flashed white to 0.7 at its middle (the shader's flash, as a send-out's), the swap at the middle; never a cross-fade of two models at once |
| Getting on | 0.45 s: the mount comes out of its ball in its limited form over 0.2 s (from half its size, white to its own colours), is ridden from 0.2 s (a change of form into its running form), and the rider hops from 0.9 m beside it (on its right) to the seat between 0.1 s and 0.45 s, on an arc 0.5 m over the line between them (smoothstep) |
| Getting off (the key, a script) | 0.4 s: the rider hops from the seat down to the feet over 0.3 s, standing once down; the mount goes back from 0.15 s, shrinking to half and flashing white, gone at 0.4 s. A fade's midpoint (a battle, a door, a warp, a crossing) takes the player off at once, with nothing played afterwards |
| Shadows and reflections | as the player's: it casts into the first two shadow maps, is mirrored over water (on a preset that reflects) with its rider even while it swims (the mirror's clip plane takes what is under the water), coloured and outlined |
| The camera | while ridden it comes over to the mount over 0.5 s (smoothstep) and back as fast, and at nought it is the walker's camera to the bit. Ridden: the point looked at 2.4 m over the feet, the arm 7.4 m, pitched 18°; **sprinting** (whole from 11 m/s, nothing under 6) the arm 1.2 m longer, the lens 6° wider and the point led 1.5 m ahead along the camera's way; **gliding** 2.2 m over, the arm 8.5 m, pitched 26°, the lens 54°; **climbing** 2.2 m over, the arm 7 m, pitched 4°, so the face is seen up; **swimming** 1.8 m over, the arm 6.8 m. Between stances each number eases with a half-life of 0.3 s |

**Buildings** (OW9, `OpenBuildingKit`): a solid of a place that says how it is built (`building`: a kind of the kit, a town's way of building, storeys, the way it faces) is the grid's kit ("Buildings") at **1.5 m a tile**: a storey under a pitched roof about 2.6 m, a door about 1.9 m, beside a grown body of 1.6 m. Its width is the box's along its front in whole tiles (two at least) and its walls the box's depth (the kit's footprint a tile deeper, as the kit's always is), its front on the box's front, turned a quarter at a time to face the way its file says, its door in the bay of the map's door whose middle is nearest its front (no door where none is within 3 m of it). **It is seen from every side**: its back wall (the side's art), the roof's back fascia and the chimney's north face are drawn too (the kit's `allSides`, which the grid, always looking north, never asks), and every face is drawn from both sides, a face seen from behind lit as facing the other way (an eave from below, a gable's verge from behind). A plinth of the kit's step stone stands under its walls from the lowest ground there up to its floor. Its glass glows after dark ("The light") and the light it throws lies on the ground. A solid that says nothing of how it is built is a block of the same cut stone over its box.

**Trees** (OW9, `OpenFoliage`; decision 15): the battle's soft pine and round tree, three shapes of each, one and a half times the battle's size at size one (the battle's was made for the chibi's scale), standing at the highest ground facing up under their foot. Nearer the camera than **120 m** a tree is its mesh, drawn a transform each, nearest first, and cast into the shadow maps; beyond it, and every tree of the land beyond the region, it is an **impostor**: a card standing on its foot, turned to the camera, showing the one of eight views (a turn of an eighth each, baked once, 128 × 160 texels, lit from above and a little toward the camera) nearest the camera's side of it, turned by the tree's own way, lit by the scene's light and shadowed by nothing. A place's trees stand one by one (`trees`: a kind, a size) or in **groves** (`groves`: a polygon filled on a jittered grid of its spacing laid on the world's own grid, each cell holding a tree for its fill's share, a pine for its pines' share and a round tree otherwise, sized between its two sizes, its shape and turn by `SplitMix` of the grove's seed and the cell, so a grove is the same trees on any machine; a tree in the middle seven tenths of its cell, so neighbours never touch). Trees are scenery: the capsule walks through them, **unless their place gives them a trunk** (`trunk` on a tree, `trunks` on a grove; plan 32, after OW10, `OpenTrunks`), for a region that wants a forest walked round: each such tree is then a post of the collision, square, 0.44 m across at size one (a bit wider than the drawn trunk's foot, so the capsule's side never sinks into the bark), its side scaled with the tree's size, from 0.3 m under its foot to 4 m over it (the mount's high jump with a body over it) or 3 m times its size where that is more, four walls and no top, so nothing stands on a tree and its own foot is found on the ground; the navigation mesh is baked round them, so people and wild Pokémon go round too. A row of posts less than the capsule's width apart is a wall.

**Grass** (OW9, `OpenGrass`): tufts (the battle's soft tuft, 0.5 m across and 0.42 m tall at size one) stand on level 0's own ground where all three corners of a triangle are of the grasses or the highland and it is no steeper than 34°, three to four square metres (0.75 a square metre), each at a point of its triangle, sized 0.8 to 1.25 and turned by `SplitMix` of the chunk and the triangle; never in the water, nor within 8 cm over it in its 2 m cell or the cells beside it. They take their cover's own tuft colours (grass, light grass, deep grass, highland; root to tip, as the covers' table has them), are drawn within **22 m** of the player, shrinking to nothing over the last 5 m, sway in the wind (more in its gusts and its weather; none under REDUCE MOTION) and **part round** the player and the nearest seven figures within that reach: a tip within three quarters of a metre of a foot is bent away from it, 0.22 m out and 0.16 m down at a quarter of a metre, as the field's grass parts.

**People and Pokémon** (OW9, `OpenFigures`, `OpenFigureDraw`): someone of the map is plan 30 · FM4's grown kit in their look (their own, else their trainer class's as a battle draws it, else the table's fallback); a Pokémon is its model at its height in metres, kept between 0.35 m and 3.5 m so that none vanishes or fills the view. The **twelve nearest the camera within 45 m are live**, walking by how far they moved since the frame before (walking over 0.25 m a second, running over 3), with their outline's shell within **26 m** (a Pokémon's three levels: its model with its shell, without it, its impostor); the rest within **240 m** are **impostors**, cards of 96 texels a view (a person framed 2 m tall, a Pokémon a quarter over its height), eight views a look turned by the figure's own way, baked once a look and a look a frame into an atlas of 32 looks (the look drawn longest ago gives up its row to a new one, never a look drawn this frame); past 240 m nobody is drawn. A look whose rig or model isn't made yet is asked for and drawn once it is, never waited for. A trainer who has caught the player's eye has the field's "!" (its own art, at the kit's 1.5 m a tile) 0.15 m over the head (1.62 m for a person, the Pokémon's height for a Pokémon), popping up as the grid's does. Whoever follows the player, and every wild Pokémon a zone has brought out (plan 32 · OW7), is a figure as the people are. **A wild Pokémon that notices the player** (it senses them while roaming: a shy one turning to run, a curious one to come, an aggressive one to charge; a calm one never does) has the same "!" over its head for **one second** of the field's ticks (`OpenWildField.ExclaimTicks`), popping up as a trainer's; a curious or an aggressive one also cries out as it notices (its species' cry as it is, panned by its side of the camera's way up to two thirds over; only the first of a frame's, so a herd noticing at once is one cry), and a shy one runs off quietly.

**Beyond the region** (OW9, `OpenHorizon`): OW8's band (the table above) and past it the far land, rings round the region's rectangle at 165, 215, 275, 345, 425, 515, 615, 730, 860, 1,010, 1,180, 1,380, 1,620, 1,900, 2,220 and 2,600 m out, each sampled about as finely as it lies from the next and round its corners in quarter circles, two rings joined by walking both round together, a few thousand triangles whatever the region's size. Its hills are value noise of the map's own seed (three octaves of 430, 190 and 85 m, shaped so the low half is valley floor), rising from the band's end until by 640 m out they stand up to 72 m over the border's median, and the farthest rings lifted up to 30 m more from 900 m so the hills close the view against the sky. Each corner takes its cover by its height and its face's slope (deep grass from 25°, rock from 40°, dark rock from 63°, the highland 22 m over the median), so the covers' borders are drawn texel by texel as the region's own; the band is level grass to its light and its covers both. **Far forests** stand on it from 70 m to 1,150 m out on a grid of 13 m: a cell holds a tree where the forests' own noise is over 0.53 (nine in ten of those cells) and the land under it is gentler than 30°, a pine or a round tree by another noise so a forest is mostly one or the other, each an impostor of the trees' atlas sunk 15 cm into its ground.

#### The prototype (OW0)

What `openproto` draws, as the gate's look was judged: the same units, scene, camera and fog; per-vertex cover in the table's base colours under one grey grain, box-mapped by each face's axis; levels by the camera's distance (0.5 m cells to 70 m, 2 m to 230 m, 4 m beyond, a chunk with a carve kept fine to 260 m); the battle's soft trees as meshes within 120 m and as impostors of eight views beyond; one shadow map 70 m across round the player; the battle's tilt-shift; plan 30 · FM0's grown stand-in for the player (1.6 m) and a person (1.65 m); a Pokémon as its battle model at its species' height in metres.

### The Wild Area (plan 33 · GL2)

- **Weather by part**: each part of the Wild Area has its own weather of the day, and the picture takes the weather of
  the part the player stands in, as an area's weather on the grid does; crossing into another part changes the sky and
  what falls with it.
- **Rustling grass**: no art of its own. The tufts over the patch part as round a walker, and the walker goes round a
  circle of 0.35 m three times a second, so the grass leans this way and that; one or two patches at most are drawn
  (the shader's eight walkers keep the player and the nearest figures first), and only within the grass's reach (22 m).
- **The Stone Arch** is the land's rock (two legs and a lintel, carved as additions), shaded and covered as rock is.
- **The Wild Area's station** is a building of the Galar kit (`terminal`, its sign WILD AREA STATION); the watchtower
  is a `tower` on its knoll.

### Max Raid dens (plan 33 · GL3)

- **A den** is a ring of red stone set in the ground, about three metres across.
- **Its beam** (lit dens only): a column of soft red light rising from the ring, about two metres wide at the foot and
  fading out at thirty metres, slowly pulsing (a two-second breath); after dark it is the brightest thing in the Wild
  Area, so it can be seen across the downs. Our own. Drawn (`Graphics/OpenWorld/DenBeams.cs`) as camera-facing ribbons
  added to the scene in six lengths, each in three widths (a core a third of the column, the column, a halo two and a
  half times it), red `255,92,70` at the foot deepening to `214,36,52` at the top, the strength falling as the square
  of the height left; hidden behind hills by the scene's depth, drawn within 420 m, a boss of more stars a little
  brighter. A Wishing Piece thrown in lights the same beam.
- **A raid's battle** puts the player's four Pokémon on four platforms in an arc on their side and the boss alone,
  giant, on the other; its barrier's shields show as small pips on its HP box, one to a shield. The boss's box is a
  single foe's at the top left, the shields a tray of rounded steel plates rimmed in white hanging under it; the four
  raiders' small boxes (a triple battle's) stack down the right from 452, the player's own first. The four stand in a
  row across the player's platform, as a triple's three do.

## Time of day

The world follows the computer's clock, like the DS's real-time clock, with Platinum's five light states (pret/pokeplatinum, `src/rtc.c`). The options screen can fix a time. Each state has a light rig for the field and one for battles (`ArtLook.FieldRigFor`, `BattleRigFor`); within half an hour of a change the two rigs blend.

| Time | Hours | Field light | Field grade and extras | Battle sky (zenith → horizon) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Morning | 4–9 | pale gold sun, lower in the sky; soft blue shade | mist (fog 32 %), gentler contrast | `104,150,214` → peach `246,224,206` |
| Day | 10–16 | golden sun, violet-blue shade | as in the tables above | `78,146,226` → `224,238,250` |
| Twilight | 17–19 | low orange sun `0.90,0.56,0.32`, magenta-violet shade, long shadows | more bloom and saturation; lamps come on (60 %) and windows start to glow (45 %), their pools still faint | `54,62,128` → orange `252,168,108`, pink clouds |
| Night | 20–23 | cool moonlight `0.27,0.31,0.44` | saturation 0.80, vignette 0.40; street lamps, windows and glass doors fully lit, warm light pools on the ground under them, bloom from the lit glass | `8,14,38` → `40,58,100`, stars, dark clouds |
| Late night | 0–3 | dimmer moonlight | saturation 0.72, vignette 0.45; the lamps, shops and signs burn on, but most homes have gone dark | `4,8,26` → `28,42,80`, stars |

- Lit glass is marked in the art, texel by texel (see *Buildings*, "Lights after dark"): it turns to warm lamplight `255,204,117`, scaled by the texel's own brightness, by the rig's amounts for public lights and for homes.
- Night battles stay a little brighter than the night field and gain a stronger rim light (0.55), so Pokémon read clearly.
- Light never drops to black: the darkest ambient is about 0.15, and nights lean blue, never grey.

## Resolution (4K)

- The game's screen is **3840×2160**. Everything is still laid out in **1920×1080 layout units** (`GameEngine.VirtualWidth/Height`); the screen is drawn at `RenderScale = 2` real pixels per unit, so every size in this guide is in layout units unless it says otherwise.
- The interface is drawn through a ×2 camera straight into the 4K screen: text comes from large font atlases and shapes from signed distances, so both are sharp at 4K. Edges are anti-aliased over one real pixel, not one layout unit (`UiShapes.PixelScale`).
- Pixel art keeps its whole-number scales: a field texel is about 6 real pixels, a party icon texel is 8.
- The window shows the 4K screen scaled to fit. Full screen on a 4K display shows it 1:1; a smaller window downsamples it, which also anti-aliases it. The first run picks the largest listed window size the monitor can show. The listed sizes are 1280×720, 1280×800 (the Steam Deck's screen, plan 16 · T2: the 16:9 picture is letterboxed in it, a band of black 40 real pixels tall above and below), 1600×900, 1920×1080, 2560×1440 and 3840×2160; 1280×800 is picked only for a screen of exactly that size, which has no taskbar to leave room for.
- The full-screen window is one row taller than the display (`WindowSettings.FullscreenSize`). A borderless OpenGL window that matches the monitor exactly is taken over by the graphics driver as exclusive full screen and flickers on NVIDIA cards.

## Quality presets

| Preset | 3D scene resolution | Anti-aliasing | Shadow taps | Shadow map | Ambient occlusion | Wide depth of field | Reflections in water |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| High (default) | 3840×2160 | FXAA in a window wider than 2880; in a smaller one the downscale to the window does it | 9 | 2048 | yes | yes | yes |
| Medium | 2880×1620 | FXAA in a window wider than 2160 | 4 | 2048 | yes | yes | yes |
| Low | 1920×1080 | FXAA in a window wider than 1440 | 1 | 1024 | no | no | no |

Reflections in water (plan 09 · L10) are a pass of their own (`FrameSection.Reflections`): what stands near the view is drawn a second time, mirrored about the water's plane, into a target of 1920×1080 whatever the preset, before the scene. Where no water is in view (six tiles or fewer), outdoors or not, the pass draws nothing and costs nothing. Puddles and polished floors mirror the walker on every preset: a quad each.

The interface is always drawn at 4K; the presets change only the 3D scenes. FXAA is for a window that shows the scene at more than three quarters of its size: a smaller window smooths the scene by scaling it down, and FXAA would cost half a millisecond for nothing. Options are saved in `settings.json`.

A frame must stay under 8 ms on High. In the harness's 1080p window after G11 (plan 04): towns and routes 5.0 to 6.6 ms, a room 3.6 ms, battles 6.0 to 7.5 ms with a move's effect at its height; Medium takes 0.4 to 1.1 ms less and Low 1 to 2 ms less. One millisecond of each of those is the hidden window's own swap of its buffers (three in a window as large as a 4K display, where FXAA adds 0.4 more), and readings move by two or three tenths between runs. The harness's `profile` mode says where a frame goes; what was learned from it:

- A frame is spent filling 4K, not on geometry: a few hundred thousand triangles and a hundred or two meshes cost a tenth of what shading every pixel does. An effect is weighed by what it adds per pixel.
- A 4K texture is slow to read at scattered places. What needs the picture or its depth small (the wide blur, the glow, ambient occlusion) reads a half-resolution copy made once a frame.
- A scene seen from low down (a battle's stage) lays its depth down before it is shaded, so each pixel is shaded once.

**The first run's measurement** (plan 16 · T23). High is the default only where the machine keeps it. On the first run (no `settings.json`) the game renders the title's three places, as the notice already warms them, behind the first start's page and the notice, which stays until it is done: from High down, ten frames to settle and thirty timed at each preset, each from a card with nothing left to do to a card that has done it all, less what streaming cost the frame. It starts on the highest preset whose median is under **6.5 ms** (the heaviest town's frame on High on the GTX 970, so a battle's extra millisecond still fits the 8 ms budget), and on Low where none is. The Quality row's help then says what was measured ("Measured on this computer at the first start: …"); a preset chosen on the page meanwhile is the player's and stays.

**Dynamic resolution** (plan 16 · T23), off unless chosen, and switched on by the first run only where even Low misses the budget. The 3D scene is drawn at **100, 90, 80, 70, 60 or 50 %** of the preset's width and height, into the top-left part of the preset's own target (nothing is made again: the half-size buffers are 960×540 whatever the preset), and composited to the whole picture; the interface stays at 4K. One frame in fifteen waits for the card and is a sample of the frame's work: a step down when the median of the last twenty is over the frame the cap allows less a quarter (12.5 ms at 60), a step up only after three seconds of samples under three fifths of it, never two steps within a second. The outlines keep their width on the screen (fewer of the smaller scene's texels), and FXAA is asked by the part's width. Where the card can't be waited for, the row says so and stays off; the harness never scales unless `SHOTS_SCALE` holds a step.

**Other shapes of screen** (2026-11-12; rulings, "Other shapes of screen, and the resolution"). The interface is laid out in its 1920×1080 **safe area** and never moves or scales for the window's shape; the picture round it is a **canvas** of the window's shape in the same units, 1080 tall where the window is wider than 16:9 (21:9's 2560×1080) and 1920 wide where it is narrower (4:3's 1920×1440), held between 4:3 and 32:9 with bars beyond. A window within 1 % of 16:9 is exactly today's picture. The 3D scene fills the canvas: wider sees more to the sides at the same vertical field of view, narrower more above and below with the vertical field of view widened by the canvas's height over 1080 (in tangents), so the safe area shows the 16:9 view to the texel. What lies over the scene (weather, a cave's dark, flashes, fades, the cinema bars, the ways into a battle) covers the canvas; menus that cover the screen stay in the safe area, the shared teal backdrop and a prompt's dim spreading over the whole canvas, other backdrops over black. Every target the scene uses (the scene, its half-size copies, the mirror) takes the canvas's shape at the preset's height; at a full-screen RESOLUTION below the monitor's the scene is drawn into that much of its target, as dynamic resolution's steps are.

## Interface (vector)

### Typography

- **Nunito** (SIL Open Font License, see `CREDITS.md`) in three weights: Bold for running text, ExtraBold for labels and dialogue, Black for names, numbers and buttons.
- Scale (layout units): 18–20 captions and tags · 24–26 small labels · 30–32 numbers and move names · 36–42 names, prompts and dialogue · 52 screen titles · 60 the FIGHT button.
- Tracking +0.8 up to 20 px, +0.4 up to 39 px, 0 above. Level reads as a small muted "Lv" before a large number.
- **A second scale for the lines that are read** (plan 12 · Q8, the options' TEXT SIZE, Normal unless chosen): under *Large* the field's text box writes at 46 on rows 62 apart and stands 300 high, so it holds four rows where the 40-high writing of Normal holds three (a line written to fill Normal's three never runs out under it), and the battle's message box writes at 46 on rows 50 apart and takes three rows where Normal takes two (its "What will … do?" at 46 too). Nothing else changes size: menus, labels and buttons keep the scale above, because every screen lays out in 1920×1080 units to its edges and a larger interface would push panels off the picture. The answers' panel stands on the box as it does at Normal.
- No drop shadows on text over panels; white text on coloured buttons gets a 2 px shadow at 20 % black.
- **Beyond Latin-1** (plan 17 · N5, the Latin half): a character is drawn as itself if Nunito has a glyph for it, in the same weights, size and tracking as the rest of the line. The first face is Latin-1 as it always was (English and every text within it pay nothing). A **second face** of the same typeface and atlas size (160 px, mipmapped) holds Latin Extended-A (Romanian ă â î and the cedilla ş ţ, Polish, Czech, Turkish, Hungarian, the Baltic languages, French œ), the comma-below letters Ș ș Ț ț that Romanian proper uses, the curly and low quotation marks „ ” ‘ ’ and the guillemets, the euro and the trade mark; it is made the first time a text holds one of them. A text is cut into runs by the face that has each glyph and the runs are set one after another on one baseline, the tracking between two runs as between two letters, so mixed text looks like one line. Nunito lacks ĳ and ſ, the arrows and the gender marks: they stay a question mark (the marks are drawn as shapes, as before). The no-break space and the narrow no-break space (French sets one before ? ! : ;) are drawn as the plain space's width and are not break points. The title's lettering stays Latin-1 and é.
- **Faces beyond Latin: Japanese, Korean, Chinese** (plan 17 · N5, the CJK half): a **third face**, Noto Sans CJK Bold (Noto Sans SC with Noto Sans KR's Hangul, SIL OFL; `CREDITS.md`), draws every CJK character (the kana, the ideographs, Hangul, the CJK and fullwidth marks) in all three of the interface's weights, since it ships one weight only, against its size. It is set at the same size and tracking as the Nunito around it: an ideograph stands about 0.69 of the size wide and a little taller than a capital, which is how CJK text reads beside Latin. Its runs are dropped onto Nunito's baseline (its own falls about 0.05 of the size lower), so a mixed line keeps one baseline. Han glyphs take the Simplified Chinese forms in every language for now: one face, cut to what the game's files use, is the build's whole cost (about a megabyte). It is loaded in **batches** of at most 600 codepoints, one atlas each at 72 px (mipmapped, 2048 px square), gathered from the chosen language's own files, every language's own name and the CJK marks, so a language's atlases are the same every run and English loads none. A CJK character is drawn as itself if the face has it; one no file of the language holds is the question mark.
- **Breaking a line of CJK**: a line breaks at its spaces, and between any two characters where either is an ideograph, a kana or a CJK mark, as Japanese and Chinese are written without spaces; never before a closing mark (、。，」』）！？ー, the small kana) nor after an opening one (「『（). Korean, which puts spaces between its words, breaks at its spaces only. Latin text breaks exactly as it did. The CJK pseudo-language (`xx-cjk`, for the harness) marks every string ［あ字한…］, so every screen shows the face and its breaks.

### Colour tokens (`ModernUi`)

| Token | Value | Use |
|---|---|---|
| Ink | `36,44,68` | text |
| Muted | `110,120,148` | secondary labels |
| Frame | `52,64,96` | panel borders |
| Panel | white → `234,240,248` | panel fill (top to bottom) |
| Shadow | `14,22,46` at 31 % | drop shadows |
| Red / Gold / Green / Blue | `232,72,76` / `240,176,56` / `56,182,104` / `70,136,232` | FIGHT / BAG / POKÉMON / RUN, and accents |
| Track | `62,72,98` | bar backgrounds |
| HP | `70,214,110` > 50 %, `246,196,50` > 20 %, `240,72,64` | HP fill |
| EXP | `72,176,250` | EXP bar |
| Selection | `240,104,70` | selected card border and glow |
| Type colours | `Palette.GetTypeColor` (Sound, our own type of plan 46: teal `64,168,152`, apart from Ice's paler blue-green and Grass's yellow-green) | type pills, move bands |
| Party backdrop | `44,112,146` → `24,60,98`, 28° stripes of 5 % white | Pokémon menu |

### Shapes and components

- Everything is drawn by `UiShapes` (signed-distance rounded rectangles anti-aliased over one real pixel, vertical gradients, borders, slanted sides and blurred shadows; rings, round-ended lines and triangles for icons and arrows; `Glow`, a soft ellipse of light that fades from its middle to nothing at its rim). No raylib rounded rectangles, triangles or lines in the kit: their edges are jagged.
- The components live in `ModernUi` (`Panel`, `Card`, `Button`, `HpBar`, `Bar`, `ExpBar`, `TypePill`, `StatusPill`, `Tag`, `Portrait`, `Hints`, `Prompt`, `Badge`, `Dim`); a screen composes these and adds no drawing of its own beyond text.
- Translucent is a colour, never a hole: a translucent panel, plate or shine is laid over what is under it, and the picture itself stays opaque everywhere, because the window shows it over black. The 2D pass writes no alpha at all (`GameEngine.Draw` masks it), so nothing drawn there needs to care.
- Panels: radius 22–34, 4 px Frame border, shadow blur 22 at offset (0, 8).
- Cards (anything that can be chosen: party slots, option rows, title entries): a panel whose selected state is a 6 px Selection border and a glow; gold instead while a Pokémon is being moved. An empty slot is a faint white outline.
- Pills: types in the type's colour, statuses as three letters (PSN, BRN, PAR, SLP, FRZ, FNT) in the status colour, short tags ("IN BATTLE") in a token colour. Gender is a vector mark after the name, blue ♂ or pink ♀.
- Icons (`UiIcons`): our own one-colour signs built from the same shapes, white on a coloured chip; on a selected row the chip turns white and the sign takes the colour.
- Key hints: a white key cap and a label on a translucent pill, right-aligned at the top of a screen (on a Frame-coloured pill when they sit on a light panel). **The cap follows the player's hand** (plan 12 · Q4, `InputManager.LastDevice`): after the last touch was the pad, the cap names the pad's button for the action (A, B, X, Y, Start, L, R, ZL, ZR, "D-pad", lettered as an Xbox pad is, the face buttons by where they lie) in the same cap and the same size, and after a key it names the key. The names are the string table's (`padButtons.*`, `shared.keys.*`), asked through `Text.Of`, which swaps a key's name for the pad's (`Prompts`); a line that names a control inside a sentence says `{key:confirm}` (`{KEY:confirm}` for the title's capitals) and gets the name of the device in use. No screen writes a device's name itself. **Drawn by action** (plan 12 · Q5): a screen asks for the hint of an action's key (`ModernUi.Hint("shared.keys.z", …)`, `HintOf`), never writes a key. On the keyboard the cap names the key the player has bound to that action (the table's string while the default key is still among its keys, else its first key; an unbound action says "None"; the arrows say the first keys of the four directions once any is rebound); on the pad the cap is **the button's sign** instead of its letters, drawn by `PadGlyphArt` at the cap's height (40): the face buttons as discs 34 across lettered A, B, X and Y in Green `64,168,96`, Red `222,76,72`, Blue `70,132,226` and Gold `236,184,52` on a 2 px ring of black at 20 %, L and R as slabs 50 by 28 (Slab to Dark, a white letter), ZL and ZR as darker slabs 56 by 30, Start as a Dark pill 58 by 26 with three white bars, Select as a Dark pill with two overlapping squares, and the d-pad as a cross of two Dark bars 34 long with the arms that count lit in Gold at their tips (all four for the arrows, the two sides for left and right, the two ends for up and down). A cap that carries a sign is as wide as the sign plus 8; a cap of letters keeps its own width. The sign is the button of the action under the preset in force and the player's own bindings.
- **The pointer** (plan 12 · Q6, Q7; `ModernUi.PointerArrow`, `UI/Kit/UiPointer.cs`): the game draws its own arrow in place of the system's, over everything (the console and the notices included): a white arrowhead 24 wide and 30 tall from its tip, rimmed 2.5 in Ink, with a short white tail 4.5 thick rimmed 9 in Ink running down and to the right from its middle, on a soft shadow of black at 27 % 3 down and 4 to the right. It is shown only once the mouse has moved or clicked over the picture (never on the bars round it), and hides after two seconds still or at any key or button, so the keyboard owns a screen while the mouse is still; while it is used the key caps show the keyboard's names. It never draws a hover state of its own: a hover moves the screen's own cursor onto the card, row, button, answer or key under it (the selected look is the cursor's), a click is the A button on it, a right click the B button (in the field with nothing under way, the menu), the wheel the arrows up and down. What it finds is the very rectangle the screen draws (one GPU-free layout method each, `ModernUi.StartMenuRows`, `PromptButtons`, `ChoiceRows`, `CommandRect`, `MoveCardRect`, `TeamCardRect`, `TargetCard`…), after the screen has slid in; a card that can't be chosen (a greyed target, an empty slot of the team) is nothing, and a click on it does nothing. A tab or an arrow beside a box's name acts on a click alone, never on a hover. Where a line waits to be read, a click anywhere reads it on.
- Prompts: a question over the dimmed screen, a muted line of explanation, and a row of buttons; the safe answer comes first and is the one highlighted.
- Buttons: pills; gradient 18 % lighter at the top to 12 % darker at the bottom, border 35 % darker, a soft shine over the top 38 %. Selected: a 6 px white ring and a glow in the button's colour.
- HP boxes: slanted sides (skew ±0.2), name + gender left, level right, HP bar under; the player's box adds HP numbers and an EXP line.
