- Caught mark: in a battle with no trainer, a foe whose species the Pokédex has as caught has a small Poké Ball (the bag's ball icon, 0.8× the name's size) 12 after its name and gender, before its status pill, in the single box and the double battle's compact boxes alike; never on a trainer's Pokémon nor on the player's side (Platinum's `HEALTHBOX_INFO_CAUGHT_SPECIES`).
- HP bar: pill with an amber "HP" tag 2.3× its height.
- Dialogue: wide panel near the bottom, speaker in a red pill tag on its top edge (so the line itself doesn't repeat the name), a bobbing red arrow when the line is complete.
- **Seeing** (plan 12 · Q8; `Core/Seeing.cs`, `ModernUi.Seen`, the options' COLOUR VISION, TEXT SIZE and HIGH CONTRAST, all as Platinum draws unless chosen). *Colour vision* (Normal, Protan, Deutan, Tritan) remaps every colour the kit draws on its way out, in `UiShapes` and `UiFonts` and nowhere else: Machado's full-strength matrix shows what the eye loses (in linear light) and the loss is moved onto the channels it still tells apart (Fidaner's daltonising shift: red's loss into green and blue for Protan and Deutan, blue's into red and green for Tritan), and the result is scaled until that eye sees it as light as an ordinary eye sees the original (the shift moves hue, never lightness), so a grey stays a grey and a token's contrast on its panel holds. Text on panels stays **4.5 : 1** or more as the player sees it under every remap (Ink and Frame on both panel tones; the high-contrast box's white and its lightened runs); what is written large or as a secondary label holds 3 : 1 (Muted, and the text box's red, blue and money runs on the light panel, which are 40 ExtraBold). Under any remap or high contrast: **every type pill carries its type's sign** (below) left of its name, the pill wider by 0.8 of its height (a pill of fixed width shrinks its name to fit), and the move card's band shows the sign over the type's name; **an HP bar at half or under is hatched**: stripes 3 wide, 11 apart, slanted 0.6, in black at 30 % over the fill and off its round ends, one way in the yellow and crossed in the red, so the zone reads without its colour. The foe's HP numbers stay hidden, as Platinum keeps them. *High contrast* draws the field's text box and the battle's message box as opaque `12,14,22` panels with a 4-wide white border and white writing (the frame chosen is passed over), the red, blue and money runs lightened to read on it.
- **Type signs** (`UI/Kit/TypeSigns.cs`): nineteen one-colour signs of our own, white on the type's colour, each built of circles, rings, round-ended lines, rounded boxes and triangles inside the square from −1 to 1 of its size (cut-outs in the colour behind): Normal a disc, Fire three tongues on a round base, Water a drop, Grass a leaf on its stem with its vein cut out, Electric a bolt, Ice a six-armed flake, Fighting a fist (a rounded block with three finger lines cut), Poison three bubbles, Ground three strata shortening upward, Flying three feathers fanned from one point, Psychic a ring round a pupil, Bug a round body and head with two feelers, Rock a faceted stone with a crack, Ghost a hood with two eyes cut, Dragon three claw marks, Steel a gear (teeth on a disc with its hole), Dark a crescent, Fairy a four-pointed star, Sound (plan 46 · CP2, a type of our own) a note: a round head leaning left with its stem rising from its right and a flag swept off the stem's top. No two are alike.
- Layout grid: 1920×1080 layout units (drawn at 4K), 48–64 margins, 24–32 gutters. Battle: opponent box top-left, player box right above the commands, prompt bottom-left, a large FIGHT button with BAG, POKÉMON and RUN stacked beside it, and where the options offer it the AUTO button hung on the prompt's top edge at its right end, beside FIGHT (plan 12 · Q12).

### Field menus and notices

- **Text frames** (`ModernUi.DrawTextBox`, the options' Text frame; plan 08 · P2): the box lines are written in has six frames of our own, all drawn with `UiShapes`, none pixel art from the games, and all the same size and place so no line breaks differently in one. *Plain* is the box as it has always been (the panel, radius 32). *Double rule* adds a second 2-wide rule in Frame colour 12 inside the panel's edge. *Wood* is a 10-wide edge of `122,82,46` with a thin `176,128,78` rule inside it. *Stone* is a 10-wide edge of `112,118,130`, radius 10, with a rule of `168,174,186`. *Dark glass* is a translucent `24,30,52` panel with a `120,150,210` rim and the writing in `236,242,255`. *Ribbon* is the plain panel with a Red bar 16 high across the middle of its top and its bottom edges. The speaker's tag, the arrow and the answers' panel are the same in all six.
- **Text box markup** (`UI/Kit/RichText.cs`, `ModernUi.DrawRuns`, `DrawBacklog`; plan 09 · L3): a line may carry codes in braces, which the box reads and never shows. `{red:…}`, `{blue:…}` and `{name:…}` set what is inside in a colour: Red `232,72,76` (the speaker tag's, for a name) and Blue `70,136,232`, each a tenth deeper (`ModernUi.RunInk`) so it reads on the light panel; `{money 300}` writes the amount as a P and the number with thousands marked, in Gold darkened three tenths (the mark is a letter, not the crossed P of the interface's money: the known gap); `{pause 0.4}` holds the typing for that many seconds, `{speed 2}` multiplies the pace from there to the line's end, `{br}` breaks the row, `{sfx select}` plays a sound as the typing reaches it. Anything else in braces is no markup and stays as written. **The player's name is a coloured run in every talk** (`DialogueManager.Prepare`): blue for a boy and red for a girl, Platinum's convention; `{rival}` and `{assistant}` stay plain, and the speaker's tag, the location sign and the headless host's transcript keep names plain. A line wraps and is measured by what it reads, codes taken out (`RichText.Plain`), so no code moves where a row breaks. A pause is part of a line's typing time: the options' text speed scales the characters and not the pause's seconds; the confirm button writes a line whole and passes its pauses and sounds. The battle's message box takes no markup.
- **The backlog**: Up while a plain line is whole (a question's Up is its cursor's, so a question never opens it) opens the last fifty lines written (`DialogueLog`) over the dimmed field: a panel 70 from the top and the text box's sides apart, five rows 112 high, the speaker in 22 Black Red over the line in 30 ExtraBold, the newest in Ink and the older in Muted, a 6-wide track at the right with a Selection thumb once there are more than five. Up and Down move the window by a row, A or B puts it away; the line waits as it was. Portraits (plan 09, decision 1) are not drawn.
- **Start menu** (`StartMenu`): a panel 440 wide on the right that slides in. Platinum's order (Pokédex, Pokémon, Bag, the player's name, Save, Options), then CLOSE, then QUIT GAME under a rule. Each row has its icon on a chip; the selected row is a Selection-coloured pill with white text. **BOXES** (plan 12 · Q13; the options' BOXES ANYWHERE row, on under the modern rules) is a row of its own after POKéMON, once the player has a Pokémon: a chip of its own blue (86 132 210) with three small crates, and its A button opens the PC's box screen over the field wherever the map allows it (`Map.NoBoxLink` refuses with the error sound and a notice, "The boxes can't be reached from here."). **CAMP** (plan 10 · F6) is a row after POKéMON while the options' FOLLOWER row is on and the player has a Pokémon: a chip of its own orange (214 120 52) with a tent on a line of ground, and its A button opens the camp (below, "Menu screens (G10)").
  - **What to do next** (plan 12 · Q14): while the options' WHAT TO DO NEXT row is on and the region has something to say, a second, smaller panel hangs 16 under the menu's, as wide and sliding with it: the caption NEXT (the kit's small label) and the story's line under it in 24 ExtraBold Ink, wrapped to the panel and cut at four lines, so a line is written to fit four (`ModernUi.NextLine`, `NextPanelHeight`). The Trainer Card's front shows the same in the free corner between the score and the portrait's plate, and the save panel as a band of its own under the summary, which the panel grows by, above the rule; the title's CONTINUE card and its slot cards carry none. With the row off nothing is drawn and the panels are as they were.
  - **REQUESTS** (plan 12 · Q21, `Story/Requests.cs`): while the options' REQUESTS row is on (on unless turned off) and the player has heard of an errand (or the WHAT IS LEFT HERE row is on, whose THIS AREA tab is on the page), a row of its own after the player's name: a chip of its own magenta (204 84 160, `ModernUi.RequestColour`) with the kit's letter. With the row off, or before any errand is known, the menu is as it was.
  - **What is left here** (plan 12 · Q22, `Models/AreaChecklist.cs`): while the options' WHAT IS LEFT HERE row is on and the field is outdoors or in a room with something to count, one more small panel hangs 16 under the last one (the NEXT panel, or the menu's), as wide and sliding with it: the caption LEFT HERE and one line under it in 24 ExtraBold Ink, the three counts in a fixed order, "Items 2 · Trainers 3 · Pokémon 5" (`ModernUi.LeftHerePanel`), or "Nothing left here". Names are never listed. With the row off nothing is drawn.
- **A request pinned** (plan 12 · Q21, `ModernUi.PinnedRequest`): the one open request the requests page has pinned stands at the top left of the field, where the location sign drops in: a panel of the kit (radius 30) 48 from the left and 40 from the top, as tall as the start menu's small panels (102), as wide as its longer line and 124 (no wider than 900). At its left, 46 in, the pin of the kit in white on a Gold chip of radius 22; from 92 in, the request's title as the kit's caption (20 Black Muted, 18 from the top) and its next step under it in 24 ExtraBold Ink (48 from the top), with `{player}` filled in. It is drawn over the field only, under the text box, while no script runs and the location sign isn't showing (the sign comes first and the pin is back once it has lifted away); it has no motion and no sound. A request done, or the row off, draws nothing. **The giver's bubble**: as the player comes into the place of the pinned request (a step across into its area, or a warp onto its map), whoever its next step is taken to (a clown for a coupon, the giver for the last step) shows the note bubble (`EmoteBubble.Note`) for 1.6 s.
- **Leaving the game**: QUIT GAME asks "Quit the game?" with KEEP PLAYING (highlighted), SAVE AND QUIT and QUIT. The title menu has a QUIT entry too, with no question because nothing can be lost there. The window's close button (plan 12 · Q1, `ClosePrompt`) asks "Close the game?" in the same prompt (`ModernUi.Prompt`: the screen dimmed, the panel rising 60 into place over 0.18 s) over whatever is on screen, which stands still under it: KEEP PLAYING (highlighted), SAVE AND QUIT and QUIT where the game can be saved (in the field with nothing under way, as the start menu's SAVE), and KEEP PLAYING and QUIT anywhere else (a battle, a scene, a menu, the introduction), with a line that says the game can't be saved now. B is KEEP PLAYING. On the title screen before any game has begun (and the options opened from it) the close button ends the game at once, as its QUIT does. Esc is never the close button: it is "back". In a battle at its menu (plan 12 · Q25) that the field began and no scene goes on from, the question offers KEEP PLAYING (highlighted), SUSPEND AND QUIT (Green, where SAVE AND QUIT stands) and QUIT, and says the battle will be there to take up from the title; a battle a scene goes on from, or one at a line, offers KEEP PLAYING and QUIT as before.
- **Location sign** (`LocationSign`): on arriving outdoors (entering a town or route, stepping out of a building) a slanted plate with the place's name drops in at the top left, holds 2.4 s and lifts away. Its colour bar says what kind of place it is: green for routes, blue for lakes, gold for towns and cities. Rooms have no sign. In a region with seasons (Unova, plan 22 · U3) the plate is 132 tall rather than 96 and a second line under the name says the season (SPRING, SUMMER, AUTUMN, WINTER), 28 tall in Bold and Muted, its left edge on the name's; everywhere else the plate is as it was. While the options' WHAT IS LEFT HERE row is on (plan 12 · Q22) the plate grows by 36 for one more line, under the name or under the season, in the same 28 Bold Muted: the counts of what can still be had in the area from where the player stands, "Items 2 · Trainers 3 · Pokémon 5", in that order; a place with nothing left says so ("Nothing left here"). The plate is as wide as its widest line. With the row off the plate is as it was.
- **The crossing to another region** (plan 18 · W5; `common.Crossing`, `travel go`): no screen of its own, only the field's own pieces in order. The attendant's lines and the question (Yes and No, as every question in the field) are said on the pier; a yes and one more line, then the script's black screen closes over the field in 0.8 s (plain black, the script's fade, nothing drawn on it). In the dark the field's music stops and the vessel is heard, a ship's or a ferry's horn (`crossing_horn`, two long low blasts) or a plane's engines (`crossing_engine`); the one line of the voyage is written in the text box over the black with no speaker's pill (`crossing.voyage.<vessel>`, naming the region arrived in), and nothing else is shown: no vessel, no sea, no map, so a crossing looks the same whichever two regions it joins. As the line is put away the arrival's own transition runs under the black and the new region's theme begins; the dark then opens in 0.8 s on the arrival tile with the region's name sign already dropping in at the top left, as on any arrival outdoors. A region whose crossing should show a deck to walk on builds it as a map of its own (a link's `Scene`, not built yet).
- **Notices** (`Toast`): one line in white on a dark pill at the top centre ("Game saved."), 2.6 s; a notice that replaces another does not slide in again.
- **A medal won** (plan 13 · V9, `MedalNotice`, `ModernUi.DrawMedalNotice`; only while the options' MEDAL NOTICES row is on, off unless chosen): a panel of the notice colour `36,44,68` at 245, radius 44, 88 high, its top 112 from the screen's so it never meets a toast (28 to 96), centred, as wide as its longer line and 168 (no narrower than 420). At its left, 52 in from its edge, the medal: a disc of radius 30 in Gold with a 4-wide rim of Gold darkened three tenths and a white star of the kit (`UiIcon.Star`) in it, its two ribbon tails (Red to the left, Blue to the right, 10 wide, slanted out) hanging 14 under it from behind. Beside it, 100 from the panel's left, MEDAL and the category's name in 20 Black Gold over the medal's name in 30 ExtraBold white. It drops in from the top edge in 0.3 s with the medal fanfare (`MusicRole.FanfareMedal`), holds 3.2 s and lifts away in 0.3 s; medals won together come one after another. It waits while anything but the field and its text box is on screen. With the row off nothing is drawn or heard, and the medal is still kept.
- **The run timer** (plan 13 · V10, `ModernUi.RunTimerCorner`; only while the options' RUN TIMER row names a category, off unless chosen): a panel of the kit (`Panel`, radius 24) 340 by 144 at the top right, 32 from the edges, where the Safari Game's panel stands, and 16 under such a panel (its top at 144) while one counts something there (the Safari Game, the Bug-Catching Contest, the Catching Show, Battle Points, Castle Points). RUN TIME as the kit's label at 16 from its top, the real time under it in Black 36 Ink (`1:02:03.4`, minutes and seconds alone under the hour, a tenth of a second after the point), the game's own time under that in Bold 20 Muted (`In game 1:01:58`), and on its last line the last split the category counts: its name in Bold 20 Ink at the left (set a point smaller at a time, down to 14, until it fits) and the real time it was taken at in Black 20 Ink at the right. A finished run (its first Hall of Fame entered) says FINAL TIME in Black 20 Gold and holds its time in Gold. It is drawn over the field and its text box under the start menu and the skip's pill, and over a battle only while its commands, its moves or a line are up (the bag, the cards and the panels that reach the top right cover nothing of it then). No motion, no sound, no input. With the row off nothing is drawn; the timer still runs and takes its splits. The PC's Hall of Fame writes the run's time beside a team's day (`TIME 4:12:09`, Black 26 Muted) where its record has one.
- **The streamer kit's two rows** (plan 13 · V24): STREAM FEED (Off, A file, File + LiveSplit) and STREAM-SAFE (Off, On) stand at the options' end, after PACKS and before HELP, off by default. With STREAM-SAFE on a trainer ID is drawn as five dashes (`-----`) wherever it is shown: the Trainer Card's band, the Hall of Fame record and ceremony and a Pokémon's trainer memo; nothing else on those screens moves.
- **FRIEND AS PARTNER** (plan 13 · V20, `OptionRow.Coop`): Off and On, off by default, after STREAM-SAFE and before HELP. On, a battle beside a partner is first asked about in the field, a line of the text box with YES and NO beside it as any question has them ("Will a friend choose for Barry in this battle?"), YES first; NO, or the row off, asks nothing more and the partner chooses for themselves.

- **A report and a replay** (plan 16 · T20): F8 writes a report's folder and says so on a notice ("A report to replay was written to repros/20261026-101500."), which drops in only once the frame's picture has been taken for the folder, so it is never in its own `screen.png`. A replay in the game's window (`--replay`) shows the same pill held still at the top centre (`GameEngine.StandingNotice`): what is being replayed and which keys work for its first three seconds, then nothing; at the end the recording's end or the second it parted at, held until Menu leaves.
- **Autosave** (plan 12 · Q3, `ModernUi.AutosaveDisc`): while the options' AUTOSAVE is on, the game writes itself quietly (see "Autosave" in CLAUDE.md for the moments) and shows only a little disc in the bottom left corner, 64 from the left and bottom edges to its middle: a disc of radius 26 in the notice colour `36,44,68` at 215 alpha, a 3-wide rim in Selection, and in it three dots going round at radius 15, the head 4.5 in a full Selection and the two behind it smaller (3.5 and 2.5) at 150 and 80 alpha, two turns in its 1.6 s. It fades in over the first tenth and out over the last fifth. It has no words, no sound and no notice, takes no input, and is drawn over everything but the transition; with the option off nothing is drawn. The bottom left is free of the Pokétch (bottom right), the location sign and the Safari panel (top), the skip ring (top right) and the notices (top centre).
- **Questions in the field** (`ChoiceBox`, `ModernUi.DrawChoices`; plan 02 · S1): a question is a line of the text box like any other, written out at the text speed, with no arrow to go on once it is whole. Its answers then slide in from the right on a panel of their own that stands on the right end of the text box (its right edge the box's, 22 above it): a row 72 high to an answer in 34 Black, the panel as wide as its longest answer and no narrower than 260, the answer under the cursor a Selection-coloured pill with white text as in the start menu. "Yes" is first and under the cursor when it opens; the cancel button answers "No", or picks the last entry of a longer menu, which is where a way out goes. A menu holds six answers at most. The panel slides away as the answer is given and the box goes on.
- **Phrases** (`PhraseEntry`, `PhraseScreen`, `ModernUi.DrawPhrases`; plan 08 · P9; Platinum's easy chat, with word lists of our own): an answer of two words a script asks for (`phrase`) is chosen on one panel over the field, which stays under it dimmed by 150 as under the start menu; the screen's title is "Your words" and the hints are Z choose, X back, Enter to OK. The panel is 1320 by 820, its top 160 from the screen's, rising 60 into place over 0.2 s. Along its top stand the phrase's two words, each in a slot 480 by 110 (the slot's own colours, its rim Selection while the cursor or the word being chosen is there, a word in 44 Black in the middle, an empty slot a short rule under its middle), and OK beside them, 240 wide, Green once both words are chosen and grey `150,156,174` before. A rule 4 high is 176 under the panel's top; under it either the eight groups, two to a row in cells 612 by 96 with their names in 34 and the count of their words at the right (a group with no words is greyed), or, once a group is open, its name in 32 ExtraBold Muted and its words three to a row in cells 400 by 80, five rows showing, the word under the cursor a Selection pill with white text and a scroll bar 10 wide at the panel's right edge when there are more rows. A line in 30 ExtraBold Muted along the panel's foot says what the cursor asks for. Choosing a slot opens the groups on the group of the word already there; choosing a word fills the slot and goes on to the next empty one, or to OK; OK with a word missing is refused with the error sound; B goes back a step, and from the slots puts the panel away with the phrase as it was. The groups are the Pokémon seen (National order), the team's moves, the types, then Feelings, People, Places, Time and Little words, our own lists in `ui.json`'s `phrases.words`; a word is kept by its key (`feelings:happy`), so a phrase is said in whatever language is chosen.
- **A field move's cut-in** (plan 02 · S2): once "X used Cut!" has been read, a band of the dark notice colour `36,44,68` opens across the screen 300 high, its middle a little above the screen's, edged top and bottom with a stripe of Selection four high; the Pokémon's front sprite (the menu's, at twice its size) runs in from the right, holds in the middle a little left of centre while the move's name stands beside it in 52 Black on a slanted plate, and runs out to the left; the band closes behind it. 0.18 s to open, 0.3 s in, 0.45 s held, 0.25 s out, 0.12 s to close: about 1.3 s in all, and the script waits for it. Whatever the move does (a tree falling, a rock breaking apart) happens as the band closes.
- **The Pokétch** (`PoketchView`, plan 02 · S2): the handheld's lower screen as a watch the player wears, shown over the field at the bottom right when the Pokétch button is pressed and put away the same way; the player goes on walking under it. A body 456 by 368 in the Frame colour with a rim a shade lighter, radius 40, standing 32 from the screen's right and bottom edges, a button on its right side (the original's, which changes the app); its screen 360 by 296 is an LCD of our own, a pale green `176,204,160` drawn in one dark green `40,62,48` and a middle green `112,146,104`, in blocks of 8 so it reads as a coarse display, never anti-aliased. It slides up in 0.2 s. The Color Changer swaps the three greens for one of seven other sets of three (yellow, orange, red, purple, blue, teal, white: `ModernUi.LcdColors`), each as far from its paper to its ink as the greens are. Taken in hand (plan 06 · R14b) the player stands still and a cursor stands for the stylus: four corners of ink a block thick round the button it is on, a block outside it, never over what the button shows. Everything an app draws keeps to the blocks of 8 on the screen's 45 by 37 (`PoketchAppState.Columns`, `Rows`), in the three tones and the seven-segment figures; words are Black 24 or 28 in the ink. A Pokémon on the Pokétch is its menu icon in the screen's tones by each pixel's brightness, as the original turns its icons into the LCD's shades: dark in the ink, the middle in the middle tone, light in the middle tone faintly. The apps:
  - **Digital watch**: the hour and the minute in seven-segment figures 96 tall, a colon that blinks each second, and the day of the week under them; a touch turns the backlight on or off, the paper a quarter of the way to white.
  - **Pedometer**: the steps taken since it was last reset, in the same figures, five digits, STEPS under them and a RESET button 15 blocks by 6 at the bottom, filled with the middle tone for a fifth of a second as it is touched.
  - **Party status**: the team in two rows of three, each Pokémon's menu icon over an HP bar of its own in the LCD's greens; a fainted one is dark, one with a status has a small mark; one touched hops two blocks and lands in 0.4 s.
  - **Analog watch**: a round dial of blocks 33 across in the middle tone, a block of ink at each hour and two by two at the quarters, the minute hand 14 blocks long and the hour hand 9 drawn block by block from a middle of two by two; a touch lights the paper for half a second.
  - **Calendar**: the month's name at the top, then the days in rows of seven from Sunday, each a cell 6 blocks by 5 with its number (Sundays in the middle tone); today's cell is filled with ink and its number in the paper, and a marked day is filled in the middle tone, its number in ink.
  - **Link searcher**: TOUCH TO SEARCH FOR PEOPLE NEARBY; while it searches, two rings of blocks spreading from the middle under SEARCHING...; then the number found in the seven-segment figures over NOBODY NEARBY.
  - **Calculator**: ten places across the top (rows 1 to 5), each a figure 3 blocks by 5 a block apart from column 5, its unlit cells shown faintly as an "8" of the middle green at a low alpha, so the display reads as an LCD's; the point and the minus sign take a place each, and the error is an "E" in all ten. The operation waiting (+, -, × or ÷, also 3 by 5) stands at the far left. A rule of the middle green under the display (row 7). The keys below in four rows of five, 8 by 6, a block apart (rows 9, 16, 23 and 30), laid out as the original's: 7 8 9 C / 4 5 6 + - / 1 2 3 × ÷ / 0 . =, the 0, C and = keys two wide; each an ink outline with its sign in Black 28, filled with the middle green for 0.15 s when touched.
  - **Memo pad**: a page of 36 by 35 blocks from the top left (a block in from the edges), a block to a dot, written in the ink; a rule of the middle green down column 37; at the right the eraser (rows 3 to 15) above the pen (rows 21 to 33), buttons 6 by 13, the tool in hand shown pressed in. The eraser is a block of ink 2 by 7 with its lower three rows rubber-coloured, the pen a stick of ink 2 by 7 with a point.
  - **Counter**: four figures 7 by 13 (rows 4 to 16), a block apart, centred; under them the button, 19 by 13, an ink outline round a cap of the middle green on a shadow of ink, the cap pushed down a block onto it for 0.15 s when touched.
  - **Coin toss**: a coin 11 blocks across lying in the lower middle (rows 23 to 33), an ink rim round its face: heads is a ball of our own (top half the middle green, bottom the paper, a band and a button of ink), tails the middle green with a cross of the paper. Thrown, it rises to about 20 blocks above where it lies and bounces three times lower, its face narrowing and widening as it turns (the middle green inside the rim), with a shadow of the middle green on the ground under it that narrows the higher it is.
  - **Roulette**: the memo pad's page (pen only), the arrow turning over its middle: a shaft of ink from 4 blocks behind the hub to 13 in front, a head of two strokes 3½ blocks back from the tip, and a hub 3 by 3 with a dot of the paper. START, STOP and CLEAR down the right, 6 by 8 (rows 4, 15 and 26), as a triangle, a square and a cross; a button that can't be used now is shown pressed in (STOP while the arrow is still, START and CLEAR while it turns).
  - **Dot art**: 24 dots by 20, a block each, in the middle of the screen (columns 10 to 33, rows 8 to 27) inside a frame of the middle green a block wide; its four shades are the paper, the middle green at a low alpha, the middle green and the ink. Until it is first touched it shows our own picture, a Poké Ball.
  - **Color changer**: the colour's name in Black 40 at the top (row 6), SCREEN COLOR under it in Black 24 in the middle green; the slider's eight swatches across the lower half (row 22), 4 by 6 and a block apart from column 3, each filled with its own colour's paper round a band of its own ink and outlined in the screen's ink; a track of ink under them (row 30) with a knob 2 by 3 under the colour chosen.
  - **Kitchen timer**: minutes and seconds in figures 7 by 13 (rows 10 to 22, columns 6, 14, 24 and 32) with a colon of two blocks between the pairs; while the time is being set, an arrow 7 by 3 over and under each figure (rows 6 and 24), a triangle of ink; a bell 4 by 6 at each side of the figures (a dome of ink, its mouth of the middle green, a clapper), the two swinging a block out and back at every ring; START, STOP and RESET along the bottom (row 30), 13 by 6 with their names in Black 24, START or STOP shown pressed in as the timer's state has it (STOP as it opens and while it is set or paused, START while it counts or rings).
  - **The map of Sinnoh** (shared by the Berry Searcher and the Marking Map; `PoketchMap`): a block for each chunk of the overworld, as the Pokédex's area page and Fly lay the region out, columns 0 to 29 and rows 5 to 29 (the original's map leaves out the empty north too), 30 blocks by 25 with its top left at column 7, row 6. Sea and nothing are the paper, land the middle green, towns the ink; the picture is read once from the region's habitats file and drawn as runs of blocks along each row. A hidden place (Fullmoon and Newmoon Islands, the Spring Path, the Seabreak Path) is sea until its variable says it is known, land after. Whatever stands on the map is a mark of 3 by 3 blocks centred on its cell, each block in the ink, or in the paper where it falls on a town: the player is a solid square shown 0.6 of each second, off the overworld at the place they last went in from (`PoketchContext.Outside`).
  - **Dowsing machine**: the tiles round the player, 15 across and 14 down, two blocks to a tile (columns 7 to 36, rows 4 to 31), a dot of the middle green at each tile's corner and an edge of the middle green round them; the player's tile in the ink in the middle. A touch sends a ring of ink, a block wide, out from the tile touched to 4.4 tiles in 0.6 s; items found then blink four times a second for 0.8 s, each a tile of ink, one noticed from further off ringed by one or two squares of the middle green a block out, and the ring goes out again, until the player takes a step.
  - **Berry searcher**: the map, a block of ink (the paper on a town) on each cell where a patch is in fruit, and the player. Touched, the picture comes back into focus in 0.3 s: squares of three cells, then two, then one, each square the tone of its first cell.
  - **Marking map**: the map, the roamers on the loose as a saltire, the six markers (a ring, a square, a triangle, a heart, a cross and an I-beam), the one moved last on top, and the player over everything. A marker picked up blinks four times a second until it is put down.
  - **Trainer counter**: the chain going now along the top: CHAIN in Black 22 at the top left, its species' icon under it (10 by 10 blocks, rows 4 to 13) and its count in figures 7 by 13 from column 17 (rows 1 to 13), its leading noughts not shown; three dashes of the middle green with no chain. A rule of the middle green across row 15. Under it the three best chains as a podium of buttons 13 by 16: the best in the middle (column 16, row 16), the second to its right (column 31, row 18), the third to its left (column 1, row 20), each with its species' icon over its count in figures 3 by 5; an empty record has a dash of the middle green. A record touched hops five blocks up and down in 16 frames. Every icon on the Pokétch's map apps is its menu icon read into blocks once: the ink where it is dark, the middle green where it is light, nothing where it is clear (the original turns its icons into the LCD's shades by their brightness).
  - **Friendship checker**: the team's menu icons, 64 across in the screen's tones, wander the whole screen, each turned the way it goes, bouncing off the edges and off one another; a spot of ground that can be touched is one block of the middle tone. A Pokémon showing that it likes the player has one to three hearts of 3 by 3 blocks in the ink over its head; one in the air has a flat shadow of the middle tone under where it stood, and rises and falls in whole blocks.
  - **Day-care checker**: the yard's fence along the bottom in the middle tone, rails and a post every four blocks; over it the two Pokémon left at the Day Care, icons 80 across, facing one another, with an egg of blocks between them when there is one; across the top each one's level in small seven-segment figures (4 blocks by 7) with no leading noughts, and its gender's sign in blocks after them. Touched, the picture dissolves back in from the paper in cells of two blocks over two thirds of a second.
  - **Pokémon history**: the last twelve Pokémon caught or given, four to a row in three rows, read like a page from the oldest at the top left to the newest at the bottom right; an icon 64 across in each cell of 10 by 11 blocks, and a short dash of the middle tone in a cell not filled yet.
  - **Move tester**: the target's two types at the top right and the move's type at the bottom left (under a MOVE label in the middle tone), each named in capitals between two arrow buttons of blocks; the pressed arrow fills with the middle tone. Five exclamation marks of blocks at the top left are lit in the ink as far as the move works (none for no effect, three for normal, five for four times) and faint beyond; the words for it run along the bottom.
  - **Hidden moves** (plan 12 · Q18; ours, the games have none): while the options' FIELD HELPERS row is on, the last app on the Pokétch whether anyone gave it or not (`Poketch.ShowsHiddenMoves`, never saved). HIDDEN MOVES in Black 26 at the top left with a rule of ink across row 6, and under it the field moves a wild helper does now, three rows of blocks to each from row 8 (eight at most), a one-block bullet of ink and the move's name in Black 24; NO HELPERS in the middle tone and Black 24 when none is granted. Nothing on it is touched.
  - **Matchup checker**: a meter of three big hearts at the top, lit in the ink for each heart the pair earns (flashing for the best); under it two heart-shaped fish of blocks, each a forked tail and a heart on its side with an eye of paper, swim toward one another a step for each heart, kiss under a small heart for the best match and turn their backs and swim apart for none; at the bottom each side's Pokémon in its own button, 48 across, and the check button between them with a heart in it, shown pressed while the fish swim or when there is no pair to check.
- **The Safari Game** (plan 01 · M7): while a game is on in the Great Marsh, a panel 300 by 96 at the top right, 32 from the edges, as the other panels are: SAFARI BALLS and STEPS as labels over their numbers in Black 36, side by side; a number turns Red at a fifth of its start or under (6 balls, 100 steps). It shows whenever the field does, the Pokétch and the text box included, and goes when the game ends. A game with no limit of steps (the Johto Safari Zone's, plan 20 · J8, part b, which ends only with its balls) shows SAFARI BALLS alone, on a panel 150 by 96 whose right edge stays where the wider one's is.
- **The catch streak and the chain of hooks** (plan 38 · EX3, `ModernUi.StreakCount`): where neither the Safari Game nor the Bug-Catching Contest is on, the same corner holds a panel 230 by 96 (360 with both counts): the species' menu icon at twice its size on the left, then STREAK (with the WILD POKéMON row at VISIBLE and two or more of one species caught in a row from the grass in sight) and HOOKS (two or more of one species hooked in a row, shown only while the player stands at the spot they were cast from) as labels over their numbers in Black 36, the streak's turning Gold from eleven, where its bonus begins, and the hooks' Gold from two.
- **Pennants and a timed mission's corner** (plan 41 · EG3, EG6; `ModernUi.PennantsCount`, `ModernUi.MissionCorner`): **a pennant** is drawn with shapes, never typed (`ModernUi.Pennant`): a short pole in Frame a tenth of its height wide and a red triangle (`214,82,66`, the goods stall's awning) flying from its top to four fifths of the height's width, and a count of pennants is the pennant and the number after it in Black (`ModernUi.Pennants`). The pennants held stand at the top left in the Battle Points' panel (300 by 96, PENNANTS in the small label, the pennant and the count at 36) while a person of Pennant Hall speaks of them (`plaza coins`), and go as the script ends. A timed mission under way has the Bug-Catching Contest's corner at the top right while the player is in its region, 340 wide: MISSION over done/asked at 36 Black and TIME LEFT over m:ss at 36, red in its last fifth; out of its region it isn't drawn, since its clock stands still there.
- **The Bug-Catching Contest's corner** (plan 15 · E5; `ModernUi.BugContestCount`): while a contest is under way and the player is in the National Park, the Safari Game's panel in its place and shape, 300 by 96 at the top right: SPORT BALLS and TIME LEFT as labels over their numbers in Black 36, the time as minutes and seconds (`19:58`), rounded up; each turns Red at a fifth of its start or under (4 balls, 4:00). The two are never on screen together.
- **Story scenes** (plan 02 · S1): a script has the field to itself, and the interface shows nothing of that but its text. Its fade is plain black over the field and under the text box, so a line can be read on a black screen; it takes 0.4 s unless the scene says otherwise. People a scene sends walking keep the trainers' walking pace (4.5 tiles a second, 8 in a hurry) and come to rest in an eighth of a second. A shake of the camera moves the picture up and down by whole texels thirty times a second, six texels at its start and dying away over its length (half a second unless said).
- **A scene skipped** (plan 12 · Q20; `SkipHold`, `ModernUi.SkipRing`): with the options' "Skip scenes" on `Seen` or `Any` (off unless chosen: then nothing of this is drawn or read), the menu button held for one second while a scene that may be skipped runs skips it. **The hold's ring** is all the interface shows of it: a pill of the notices' dark (`58,70,104` over `36,44,68` at 245, the white rim at 60 and 3 wide, as a notice's) 56 high, its right end 64 from the screen's right edge and its middle 60 from the top, which is the middle of a cinema bar, so over a scene's bars it sits inside the top one; SKIP in 24 Black, white, 24 in from its left end, and at its right end a ring 36 across and 5 wide, 12 in from the pill's end: a track of white at 70, and over it the fill in white, growing clockwise from twelve o'clock in step with the hold (laid as strokes with round ends, 48 to the turn). It is drawn only while the button is down and the scene may be skipped: it is there from the first frame of a press, empties and goes the moment the button is let go, and one long press skips one stretch (the button is let go before it counts again). It is drawn over the field, the script's black screen, the cinema bars, the text box and a question's answers; beside a Safari Game's panel, whose corner that is, it stands 24 to the panel's left. A question is never skipped, so nothing is drawn while one is on the screen. When the ring is full the text on the screen is put away and the whole picture goes to black in 0.2 s; behind it everyone is put where the scene leaves them; and the picture opens again in 0.3 s on the scene's end, or on whatever waits for the player (a question, a battle, a screen), which then goes on at its own pace. Nothing is heard of what was skipped; a change of music is kept.
- **The camera kit** (plan 09 · L4; `CameraRig`): what a scene does with the field's camera besides sending it to a spot. It sits over whatever camera the place has (the open country's, a cave's, the zoomed-in one, a room's), and every move eases out of where it is and into where it goes on the same curve as a pan (3t² − 2t³).

| Move | What it does | Numbers |
|---|---|---|
| Pan | the camera looks at a tile's middle instead of the player, and stays | 0.8 s unless said; from a spot it was sent to already it eases on from there |
| Follow | the camera keeps on someone else's feet, wherever they walk; `player` brings it back without touching the lens or the bars | 0.8 s to get onto them |
| Path | the camera travels through the middles of up to eight tiles, from where it looks | 4 tiles a second unless said (a quarter of a tile to 16); it gathers that pace over half a second and loses it over the last half second, and holds it between (a path too short for both halves them) |
| Zoom | the lens alone: a factor of 1 is the place's own, 2 shows everything twice as large, 0.5 twice as much ground. The field of view changes (tan of its half divided by the factor) and never the pitch or the distance, so upright things stand as straight at every zoom | 0.5 to 4; 0.8 s unless said, eased on the factor's ratio so the picture grows evenly |
| Bars | the title's own cinema bars: plain black, 120 units at the top and the bottom, sliding in and out in whole units | 0.4 s unless said |
| Slow motion | the field at a share of its pace for a time, then back by itself | a tenth to the whole of its pace, for 2 s unless said (60 at most), easing in and out over 0.2 s |
| Release | everything back: the camera to the player, the lens to the place's own, the bars away, time to its pace | 0.6 s unless said |

  The bars lie over the field, its weather and a script's black screen, and under the text box, the answers and every panel, so a line is read across the lower bar. Slow motion slows what the field does (people's steps and gestures, the player's, prints and dust, weather, a scene's effects, a puzzle's pieces) and nothing a scene is timed by: the camera's own moves, the bars, the text, a bubble, a fade and a script's waits all keep the clock's time, so a scene's lines mean the same seconds whatever the pace. A zoom out shows more ground, and what is drawn follows the lens: the ground in view, the people on it, the chunks kept ready, and the shadow map, which is spread over the wider view (its texels grow with it). In a room, which is seen whole, a zoom in lets the camera look where it is sent as far as the zoom leaves room: by the share of the room the zoom has taken out of the picture, and no further. A scene that ends, however it ends, releases what it left.
- **The console** (plan 16 · T6; `ModernUi.ConsoleSheet`, `FrameTimes`): the developer's alone, never a player's; the grave key opens and shuts it in developer mode (`--dev`), and the game waits under it. A sheet the width of the screen and 520 high from its top, `16,20,32` at 232, with a Selection rule 4 high along its foot; the line being typed 41 above that rule's top, `> ` and the line in Bold 24 white, a Selection caret 3 wide that blinks twice a second, under a hair rule of white at 50; the output above it from the newest up, a line every 30: what was typed in `160,172,200`, answers white, refusals `255,120,110`, a script's trace in Selection, each cut with an ellipsis where it would run past the sheet's 32 margins. The frame's time (`fps on`) is a pill of the same dark at 210, 48 high, 24 from the top right corner, in ExtraBold 26 white; with the profiler's passes (`fps sections`) a second pill 38 high under it, their line in Bold 20 Selection. Both stand over everything but the close button's question, and neither is in the console's own screenshot.
- **What a battle left behind** (plan 39 · CR1; `GameEngine.KeepWhatWasLeft`, `LeftBehindLine`; only while the Crafting Kit is in the bag): once the field is the player's again, with no script and no other toast showing, the field's toast (its own style: a pill sliding in from the top, `Toast`) tells in one line everything the wild Pokémon of the battle left in the pouch, the counts of one material added: "Left behind: Soft Fluff ×3, Plush Tuft ×1." Never for nothing, never inside a battle, and one line for a horde's.
- **Photo mode** (plan 12 · Q16; `Overworld/PhotoView.cs`, `GameEngine.Photo.cs`, `ModernUi.Photo.cs`): the Photo action (F9, the pad's Select) in the field with nothing under way (where the start menu could SAVE) stills the field: people, the player, prints, falling weather and a scene's life stop where they are (`FieldLife` isn't advanced), and only the grass's sway and the water go on. The camera is the camera kit's (`CameraRig.Pan`, `ZoomTo`), never one of its own: the arrows move it at 6 tiles a second up to 10 tiles east or west and 8 north or south of the player (`PhotoView.ReachX`, `ReachZ`), the page buttons zoom it a step of × 1.25 between 0.75 and 2.5 (`PhotoView.WidestZoom`, `TightestZoom`, inside the kit's own bounds for every place's camera), and leaving brings it back over 0.3 s. **The panel** stands at the top left, 32 from the edges, 440 wide: the caption PHOTO (the kit's small label), then five rows 56 high (ZOOM, TIME, WEATHER, FILTER, FRAME), each its name in Bold 24 Ink at the left and its value in ExtraBold 24 at the right between two Muted triangles 10 wide; Menu opens and shuts it, Up and Down choose a row (a Selection pill, white words) and Left and Right change it. Shut, it shows only its caption and the five values in one column of Muted 20. A weather that can't be borrowed here (a room, a cave) shows NOT HERE in Muted and doesn't change. **The hint** is a pill 52 high centred 32 above the bottom, `16,20,32` at 200, its words in Bold 22 white with each key as the prompts name it. The Item button hides the panel and the hint while a picture is composed; A takes it, and nothing of the interface is in it: what is written is the field, its frame and its filter. **Frames**: *None*; *Mat*, a border 48 wide of `246,242,232` round the picture with a hair rule 2 wide of `200,192,176` 12 inside its inner edge; *Cinema*, the camera kit's bars (120, black). B shuts the panel, or with it shut leaves photo mode.
  - **A pose and a face** (plan 43 · LF4): POSE and FACE are the panel's last two rows: the player's gesture held at 45% of its round (None, then the nine gestures, named as the card designer names them) and a mood (Calm, Smiling, Wide-Eyed, Wistful, Fierce); a follower turns to the camera. Photo mode runs no field, so the pose holds; leaving puts the face and the follower back. **At a photo spot** the camera stands where the spot puts it, the panel opens with POSE and FACE alone, and the picture taken closes it. **Every picture photo mode takes** goes into the screenshots folder at 4K as before and, while the photo book has room, into `photos/` beside the saves at 1920 × 1080 with a 480 × 270 copy for its page, with a toast "Kept in the photo book."
- **Captions** (plan 12 · Q9; `CaptionQueue`, `ModernUi.Captions`; only while the options' CAPTIONS row is on, off unless chosen): every sound effect and every cry is named on screen as it plays, in a few words of our own (`captions.` in `ui.json`: "A door opens", "Starly cries", "Tackle is used"). A caption is a pill of the notices' dark (`36,44,68` at 235, the white rim at 60 and 2 wide), 48 high and radius 24, as wide as its words and 72; in it, 20 in from its left end, a sound's sign of `UiShapes` in Selection (a small box and its cone, and one arc of a wave 4 beyond the cone; a cry's in Gold), and 52 in from the left end the words in Bold 26, white. The pills stand in a column at the left, their left edges on the text box's (96 from the screen's left), the newest at the bottom with its foot 88 above the text box's top (730 from the screen's top), clear of the speaker's tag, and the older stacked over it 10 apart; three at most, so a fourth puts the oldest away. Each holds 3 s from its sound: it fades in over its first 0.15 s and out over its last 0.4 s, sliding nothing. A sound that plays again while its caption still stands renews it in its place and counts the times after the words (`×3`, in Bold 22 Selection, 10 after them), so a run of cursor blips or a ball's wobbles is one pill. Captions stand over the field, a battle, the text box and every screen, under the transition and the close button's question, and are drawn the same in every language; they take no input. A sound dropped unheard (a scene being skipped) has no caption, and a sound muted by the volume still has one. With the row off nothing is drawn or kept. The field's ambience is named too, once as each bed comes in (`BedWatch`: its gain past 0.2, and not again until it has fallen under 0.05, so a river's bank walked along is one caption): "Rain falls", "A waterfall roars", "The cave hums softly" (`captions.bed`), the sign two waves one over the other, 16 wide and 8 apart, in Selection.
- **A tip** (plan 12 · Q23; `ModernUi.TipCard`; while the options' TIPS row holds, by the rules unless chosen, so the modern rules show them): a few words of our own on something the first time the game shows it, once a game. It comes up at the next quiet moment after what brought it, never on top of anything under way: in the field when the game could be saved there (no script, no trainer walking up, the start menu shut, the player not mid-step), and in a battle only as its main menu opens and waits, never while the log plays, one tip to an opening. The screen under it stands still and is dimmed by 110; on it a panel of the kit (radius 36) 1040 wide and 360 high, centred, its middle 40 above the screen's, rising 60 into place over 0.2 s as a prompt does. At its top left, its middle 56 in and 60 down, a Gold chip of radius 26 with the kit's star in white; from 100 in, TIP as the kit's label (20 Black Muted, 30 from the top) over the tip's title in 44 Black Ink (54 from the top); from 56 in and 140 from the top, its lines in 30 ExtraBold Ink on 42 leading, wrapped to the panel (written to fit three). The confirm key's hint (OK) stands at its bottom right, 40 in, its top 76 above the panel's foot. A or B puts it away once it has been up a quarter of a second, with the cursor sound; it comes up with the select sound. Tips that wait come one at a time. With the row off nothing is shown and nothing is kept.
- **Holding to confirm** (plan 12 · Q9; `ConfirmHold`; only while the options' HOLD TO CONFIRM row is on, off unless chosen): a dangerous answer, one that throws something away for good (DELETE A SAVE's YES on the title, RESET's YES in the options, RELEASE's question in the PC's boxes), is taken only once the confirm button has been held on it for 0.4 s. As the button is pressed the answer's button gains a ring at its right end, 24 in from the end and its middle on the button's: 36 across and 5 wide, a track of white at 70 and the fill in white growing clockwise from twelve o'clock in step with the hold, laid as the skip's ring is (48 strokes to the turn); the button stays as it was under it. Let go early, the ring goes and nothing is done; held to the end, the answer is given as a press gives it with the row off. B and the arrows are as they always were, and every other answer is a press.

### Battle panels

- **Waiting for the other player** (plan 28 · I3, a link battle: `BattleMenuState.Waiting`, `ModernUi.WaitingDots`): once this player has answered and the other hasn't, the commands go and the message box (the whole width, as for a line) says "Waiting for {name}…" in the line's own type, with no advance arrow; in the arrow's place, 120 in from the box's right edge and 46 up from its foot, three discs of 9 units, 30 apart, the lit one Red and the others the ink's grey (196, 200, 210), the light stepping one disc on every 0.4 s of the frame clock. Nothing can be pressed. The turn plays as soon as the server sends it.
- **The curtain** (plan 13 · V12, VERSUS, `ModernUi.VersusCurtain`): when the player at the controls has chosen and the other is asked, the whole picture goes to the menus' backdrop (teal with its stripes, nothing of either side's battle showing through) and a panel 1100 by 420 stands in the middle: "Pass the controller to Player 2" in Black 56 Ink, under it "Player 1, look away while they choose." in ExtraBold 34 Muted, and at its foot "Press A when you're ready" in Black 30 Red with the waiting dots at the panel's right. A lifts it on the other player's own screen at their commands. A turn plays on the screen of whoever chose last; the other's is played along unseen and silent.
- **Each player from their own side** (plan 28 · I3): a link battle's screen always stands on its own player's side, as every battle does: their Pokémon on the near platform with the full HP box and its numbers, the other's across the field with the bar alone, drawn on its stand-in's own scale from the 48ths the server sends (so it moves in Platinum's steps and says nothing finer). The lines are in their own words: "Go! X!" and "Come back, X!" for their own, "Lucas sent out X!" and "Foe X" for the other's.
- **A puzzle's goal** (plan 13 · V26, `ModernUi.PuzzleGoalLine`): over a battle puzzle's battle, at the top middle where neither health box stands, a Blue pill 60 tall (radius 30, its shadow under it), the goal in white Black 30 from 32 in ("Win this turn") and at its right "Turn n of N" in pale blue (220, 232, 255) ExtraBold 24. It stays for the whole battle, under the message box and the menus.
- **A tip in battle** (plan 12 · Q23): the field menus' tip card ("Field menus and notices", "A tip"), the same in every way, over the battle at its main menu: the commands and both HP boxes stay drawn under the dimming, and the battle is held until it is put away.
- **Whose turn it is** (plan 13 · V20, a friend in the partner's place): in a tag battle whose partner a friend chooses for, the commands' message box carries the speaker's pill over its top left (`SpeakerPill`, as a trainer who speaks has) saying whose turn it is: "Lucas's turn" while the player's own Pokémon is asked, "A friend's turn" while the partner's is. Nothing else of the commands changes; the friend's POKéMON lists the partner's team on the switching cards, and their BAG says "A partner has no bag to use in this battle!" in the message box, with the error sound, and opens nothing. In any other battle no pill stands over the commands.
- **Switching**: the field dims and the team appears as six cards, three to a row, under a one-line question. Each card has the icon, name, level, HP bar and numbers, and a tag: IN BATTLE, a status, or FNT (the card is greyed). The player's HP box is hidden behind the cards.
- **TRY AGAIN and GIVE UP** (plan 12 · Q25, the options' RETRY BATTLES row, off unless chosen): when the player has lost and the battle's last line has been read, the battle stands still under the kit's prompt (`ModernUi.Prompt`, as the close button's: the screen dimmed, the panel rising into place over 0.18 s), "The battle was lost" over a line naming whom it was against and saying that trying again starts it over from its first turn, with what was used and spent given back. Two buttons: TRY AGAIN (Green, highlighted) and GIVE UP (Red); B is GIVE UP. TRY AGAIN closes the picture to black as a battle's end does and opens on the same battle's first line, with no way into it played a second time; GIVE UP goes on as a loss always has. Never after a battle a scene lets be lost, the catching lesson, the Great Marsh, Pal Park, the Bug-Catching Contest, the Battle Frontier, or under a Nuzlocke or a run's restrictions.
- **Battle Style Shift's offer** (plan 08 · P1, the options' Battle Style): when a trainer's Pokémon has fainted and the next is chosen, the battle stops on one line in the message box that names it and asks ("Ace Trainer Vera has Starly coming out next. Will you change Pokémon?"), with Yes and No in the choice box as for a move to learn, the cursor on Yes as the original's. Yes opens the switching cards, whose question then names the Pokémon coming ("Starly is coming out next. Send out which Pokémon?") and whose hints are Send out and Stay in; backing out of them stays in. No, B or a confirm stays in. The player's HP box is hidden while the question is open, as behind the cards.
- **Bag** (plan 06 · R11): BAG opens Platinum's four battle pockets over the dimmed field, under a head panel that says BAG: HP/PP RESTORE, STATUS RESTORE, POKé BALLS and BATTLE ITEMS as buttons two to a row in their pockets' colours, each saying how many kinds it holds, and a wide grey button under them for the last item used (named on it). A pocket shows its items as cards two to a row, four rows to a page (pixel icon on a pale disc, name, ×count), the chosen one gold-edged and described on a panel to their right. An item for one Pokémon goes on to the team as six cards, three to a row (portrait, name, level, HP bar and numbers, IN BATTLE and a status), under a head that names the item; an item for one move (an Ether) then shows the evolution scene's move panel on the right (`MovePick`), titled with the question. The player's HP box is hidden behind all of these, as behind the switching cards. Item icons are 20×20 pixel art shown at 3×: the ball itself, a spray bottle whose colour says which medicine, a crystal for a Revive.
- **The bag shut** (plan 14 · B14, a Master Trainer's battle; plan 13 · V5's run rule sets the same): where nothing may be used from the bag, the BAG button keeps its place in the commands and is painted a flat grey (`ModernUi.Closed`, 150 156 174) in place of its own colour, its sign and word still white over it, so the four commands stand as they always do. Under a run with no items in battle (plan 13 · V5) the player's bag is shut the same way only where no ball could be thrown (a trainer's battle, or catching forbidden); in a wild battle BAG stays and opens, and any item but a ball is refused with the same line. Choosing it plays the error sound and says why in the message box, "No items may be used in this battle!", and the commands come back; no pocket opens.
- **A staged foe's level** (`docs/mechanics/rulings.md`, "Staged battles"; `ModernUi.Level(..., hidden)`): where a script's battle hides the foe's level, every place it would stand on the screen (the foe's HP box of any size, its target card, its card on the information panel) shows the muted "Lv" and in the number's place "???" in the same Black weight, size and Ink, right-aligned where the number's right edge would be, so the box keeps its shape. Nothing else of the box changes: its name, condition, HP bar and the trainer's tray are drawn as ever. The player's own side always shows its numbers.
- **A staged foe's lines** (the same ruling): "It can't be touched!" and "It only watches..." are battle lines like any, in the message box with the advance arrow; the move that met the untouchable foe is shown blocked on it, as a move a protection stops, with no flash or shake of the foe's body.
- **A horde's boxes** (plan 14 · B4, `ModernUi.HordeBox`): the five foes' boxes are small ones, 440 × 86 with the double's skew (−0.2) and no frame of a turn, stacked down the left from 28 at 96 apart, each 12 to the right of the one over it (`HordeBoxAt`: the fifth ends at 498, clear of the message box), sliding in from the left after the camera's sweep as a wild double's two do: the name (Black 26) with its gender, the caught mark and the condition's pill (24), the level (26) at the right, the HP bar (16 tall) under them. The player's own box is a single battle's. While the information panel is open the five stand back (the panel has a card for each), and its strip is a single battle's (240).
- **An SOS battle's boxes** (plan 14 · B5): the caller and its ally have a double's two compact foes' boxes at the top left, the second sliding in as the ally hops on; the player's own box is a single battle's. A move aimed at one asks which of the two on a double's target cards, and the move cards' hints tell of both, as a double's do; the information panel's strip is a double's (286).
- **A triple's boxes** (plan 14 · B6, `ModernUi.TripleBox`): the foes' three are a horde's small boxes stacked down the left (`HordeBoxAt`); the player's three are boxes of the same size leaning the player's way (skew 0.2) stacked down the right above the commands (`TripleBoxAt`: from 1384, 548, each 12 to the left of and 96 below the one over it, the third ending 10 above the target cards), each with the HP's numbers (Black 20) after its bar, and the one whose action is being chosen lit with the selection's frame and glow. The player's stand back behind the switching cards, the bag and a question as a single's box does, and both sides' while the information panel is open (its strip is a single battle's, 240).
- **The six cards** (plan 14 · B6): a single-target move in a triple asks where it is aimed on six cards, three to a row as the field stands (the foes on top as the stage stands them, the last place on the left; the player's three under them, the first on the left), each a horde's narrow card. Only the places the move reaches from where its user stands are lit (the foe across, the foes beside that one, the ally beside; a move that reaches anywhere, every one); the user's own card and those out of reach are greyed as the user's own is in a double. Where a move reaches one place alone it goes there without the cards. The cursor walks the lit cards as it walks a horde's.
- **SHIFT** (plan 14 · B6): for a Pokémon at an end of a triple battle's side the fourth command reads SHIFT in place of RUN, in RUN's colour, and chosen moves it to the centre as its turn; for the one in the centre it reads RUN as ever.
- **The rotation arrows** (plan 14 · B7, `ModernUi.RotationArrows`): in a rotation battle the FIGHT button carries a disc at each end, 26 in radius and 40 in from the button's side, white with a red arrow pointing out; the shoulder buttons (Q and E, the pad's shoulders) arm the turntable toward that side and the disc turns gold with an ink arrow, pressed again it goes back to the front. While one is armed the name of the Pokémon on that seat stands along the button's foot (Black 26, white), the message box names it in place of the one in front, and FIGHT opens that Pokémon's moves; the player's HP box is the front's, a single battle's. The foe's box is a single's.
- **The ring's cards** (plan 14, finishing B13): a single-target move in a Battle Royal asks where it is aimed on four cards laid out as the ring stands, three to a row: the second, at the back of the ring, alone on top in the middle, and under it, left to right, the third (on the player's left), the player's own card (greyed) and the first (on the player's right), each a horde's narrow card; the top row's ends are empty (no card). The cursor steps along the lower row past the player's own card, round it, and up and down between the middle and the nearest card that can be aimed at, as a horde's does.
- **The five and the one** (plan 14 · B4, `ModernUi.TargetCard`, `BattleEngine.TargetLayout`, `TargetColumns`): a single-target move in a horde asks where it is aimed as a double's does, on cards laid out as the field: the five foes in a row on top, left to right as the stage stands them (the last place on the left), and the player's own card under the middle one, greyed, the row's other places left empty (no card). The five share the double's width (48 to 1140) with 12 between, each about 209 wide: the portrait (28) at the left, the name fitted to the card (Black, at most 24) and the HP bar (14 tall) beside it, no level; the hint's pill hangs on each card's top right as on a double's. The cursor steps along the row and round it, passing a fallen foe's card (greyed, as a double's), and up and down to the nearest card of the other row that can be aimed at, staying where it is when there is none. On the move cards a horde's hints are against the first foe still standing; the information panel's foes' column has the five on small cards (under 140 tall: the portrait, name, condition and level, and the ability and the item on one line, `InfoCardSmall`).
- **Trainer's team**: a dark tray hangs under the opponent's box with a ball for each Pokémon (grey once fainted) and a dot for each empty place.
- A message that doesn't fit on one line wraps to two.
- **A move to learn** (plan 06 · R10): when a Pokémon that knows four moves reaches a level with another to learn, the battle stops on its question in the message box, with Yes and No in the field's choice box (`DrawChoices`) standing 22 above the message box, its right edge on the box's. Choosing which move to forget shows the evolution scene's panel (`MoveChoice`: WANTS TO LEARN, the four moves and the new one) on the right, over the field, which stays in view; the message box keeps the question. The player's HP box is hidden while the question is open, as it is behind the switching cards. B keeps every move.
- **A level** (plan 08 · P5, `LevelUpPanel`): when a Pokémon's level rises, the line that says so ("X grew to Lv. N!", with its fanfare) stays in the message box and a panel opens over the field with it, as Platinum's summary window follows its level-up line. The panel is 560 wide and 484 tall, radius 30, its right edge on the message box's and its foot 22 above it, and slides in 80 from the right over 0.18 s. Its head is the Pokémon's menu icon at 2× on a disc of radius 44, its name and gender in Black 34 and, at the right, the level it reached; under a rule come six rows 48 apart in the original's order (Max HP, Attack, Defense, Sp. Atk, Sp. Def, Speed), each stat's name in ExtraBold 30 and its number right-aligned in Black 32 under a caption. It has two pages, and a press turns each: first GAINED, what the level added to each stat as +n in Green (+0 in Muted), then NOW, the new values in Ink; the second press closes it and the battle goes on, to the moves the level brings. The numbers are the rules' own (the log's `LevelRose.Before` and `After`), never read off the Pokémon the screen shows, so the gains add up to the values and both are what the summary says afterwards. The player's HP box is hidden while the panel is up, as behind a move to learn. A Rare Candy used from the bag opens the same panel over the team's cards, on the half of the screen the Pokémon's card isn't on, its foot 22 above the prompt, which says the level's line with the arrow that waits for a press (the key hint says Next); the move the level brings and its evolution wait for the panel's two presses.
- **Move hints** (plan 12 · Q10, the `MoveHints` option): where the option is on (the default since plan 06 · R19, every game being played by the modern rules), a damaging move's card says how it will do against a foe on a pill 28 tall hung on the card's top edge (12 above it, 16 over it, so it sits in the gap between rows and over nothing on the card): SUPER EFFECTIVE in Green, NOT VERY EFFECTIVE in Gold, NO EFFECT in Muted, each in white Black 16 after its sign (an upward triangle, a downward one, a cross, drawn with `UiShapes`). A plain matchup shows nothing. In a single battle the pill stands at the card's right, 24 in; in a double battle each foe has its own as they stand, the left foe's at the left of the move's name (150 in) and the right foe's at the card's right. The target menu hangs the same pill on each card that can be chosen, 24 in from its right. Nothing is shown for a status move, for a foe whose species the Pokédex hadn't seen before the battle began, or for a place that is empty. With the option off the cards are exactly as they were.
- **A trainer's line** (plan 11 · C12, the `TrainerLines` option): where the option is on (the default since plan 06 · R19, every game being played by the modern rules), a line a trainer of the other side says in battle is shown in the message box like any other, wrapped to two lines at most, with the trainer's name (the rival's as the player named him) on a pill 44 tall hung on the box's top edge (22 above it, 22 over it), 40 in from the box's left: the name in white Black 26 on Red, 22 in on each side, a soft shadow under it as under a hint pill. The battle's own lines have no pill. With the option off nothing is said and the box is exactly as it was.
- **The information panel** (plan 12 · Q11, `BattleMenuState.Info`): the `Item` action (C, the pad's left face button) opens it from the commands; Back, A or the same button close it, back on the commands as they were. The stage dims (150) under the foes' HP boxes, which stay bright over it; the player's are hidden, as behind the switching cards. Key hints at the top right (X Close).
  - **The strip**, under the foe's box (its top 240 from the screen's in a single battle, under the trainer's tray; 286 under a double battle's two boxes), tags 48 tall from 56 in: FIELD in Frame, then the weather in its own colour (rain Blue, harsh sunlight Selection, a sandstorm a darker Gold, hail sky blue 96 168 214, fog Muted), Trick Room violet (150 96 200) and Gravity in Frame, each with its turns; NOTHING IN EFFECT in Muted when there is nothing.
  - **The sheet**, from 352 to 1032: two panels 900 wide, FOE'S SIDE (a Red tag) on the left and YOUR SIDE (Blue) on the right, as the two sides stand on the screen. Each has its side's screens, veils and hazards as tags 40 tall (what helps in Blue, what harms in Red), or "No screens or hazards." in Muted, then a card for each of its Pokémon on the field, sharing the panel's height (300 at most): the icon at 2× on a disc, the name, status and level; ABILITY and ITEM as captions over their values in Black 28, or "Not seen yet" in Muted; under them the stat stages (ATTACK +2, a rise Green and a fall Red) and the passing conditions (a help Blue, a harm Red) as tags 36 tall, or "No changes." in Muted.
  - **What it may say**: the player's own Pokémon are known whole; a partner's and the foes' show only the ability and the item a line has named, and an item the player saw go, with nothing in its place, is "None". Counts are given where nothing hidden lengthens them (Trick Room, Gravity, Mist, Safeguard, Tailwind, Lucky Chant, Heal Block, Embargo, Magnet Rise, a Wish, a Future Sight, the Perish count, the player's own screens), never for the weather or the foe's screens (a held rock and Light Clay lengthen them) nor where the rules draw the count (confusion, Encore, Disable, Taunt, a bind).
- **The item button in the move menu** (plan 06 · R20, R21; plan 25 · Y14, `ModernUi.LaterButton`): where the Pokémon whose moves are chosen can Mega Evolve, use a Z-Move or Ultra Burst, a button stands on the field, 62 tall and as wide as its word asks, its right edge at x 1216 (24 short of the player's HP box, which it would otherwise cover) and its foot 14 over the move panels: a disc 50 across at its left with the mark, the word (MEGA EVOLUTION, Z-POWER or ULTRA BURST, Black 26) and the key on a pill at its right (C, the `Item` action). Not armed, it is a white panel with a Frame border, the mark in its own colours on a pale disc and the word in Ink; armed, it fills with the mark's colour (Mega a violet-to-pink `176,92,236` to `236,104,176`, the Z-Power gold `240,176,56` to `255,214,110`, Ultra Burst gold over a dark violet `70,40,120`), the word turns white, its disc glows and a soft glow of the same colour 24 wide stands round it. **The Key Stone mark** is our own: a round stone (`236,236,246` on a rim of `120,128,160`) with two arcs of colour swirled through it (violet `186,96,255` and orange `255,176,70`), a white glint at its top left. **The Z mark** is a Z of three strokes in a diamond (a crystal, gold). **The Ultra Burst mark** is a ring of gold with eight short rays. With the Z-Power armed every move card names its Z-Move (a status move as Z- and its name), and a move that has none is greyed (`Closed`), as a card with no PP is. **Dynamax and Terastallization** (plan 25 · Y15) take the same button where nothing before them in that order can be had (Mega Evolution, the Z-Power, Dynamax, Terastallization, the order `ToggleLaterMechanic` goes by): DYNAMAX, armed red `200,30,70` to `255,96,150`, its mark our own (three chevrons rising on a red disc's rim, `232,40,84`); TERASTALLIZE, armed in the Tera Type's own colour (`Palette.GetTypeColor`, lighter to darker; a Stellar one gold `255,206,96` to violet `150,96,200`), its mark a faceted gem of six triangles in that colour, lit and shaded in turn. With the Dynamax armed, or the Pokémon Dynamaxed, every card names its Max Move (`MaxMoveAt`). **Mega Dynamax** (plan 06 · R20c) is a step of the same button: with the Mega Max Band a second press on MEGA EVOLUTION arms MEGA DYNAMAX (a third disarms both), and a Mega with its Dynamax unspent shows MEGA DYNAMAX in Dynamax's place; armed violet-pink `236,104,176` to red `200,30,70`, its mark the Key Stone inside a ring of Dynamax red `232,40,84`, 22 across and 3.5 wide, until plan 25 draws its own; the cards name the type's Max Moves, never a G-Max Move.
- **A Dynamax on the HP box** (plan 25 · Y15): while a Pokémon is Dynamaxed a pill 30 tall hangs on its box's top edge (14 above it), its right edge 40 in from the box's: red `232,40,84` with a darker rim, three small chevrons in white and the turns left (3, 2, 1) in white Black 24. The count is the rules' at every menu (`SyncFromCore`), 3 as it begins. The HP box's numbers are the raised ones from the line on, and the bar keeps its share (it neither eases up like a heal as the Dynamax begins nor shows more than the most as it ends: `BattleEngine.TakeRaisedHp`).
- **The boss box** (plan 25 · YD6; `ModernUi.BossBox`, `BattleEngine.IsBoss`): in a single battle, a foe of a Champion's Mega Max team (`SceneTransition.IsMegaMax`) that has Mega Dynamaxed has its HP box widened along the picture's top as its growth bursts (eased over 0.4 s from the burst, `ModernUi.BossShare`; at once with the scene off, since it is state of the giant standing), from the box's own 580 × 120 at 56, 52 to 1104 × 100 at 56, 22, with the same skew (−0.2) and panel. It ends at x 1160, short of the foe's platform, so a giant's head and crown stand clear of it at the overview (a box across the whole top, 1808 wide, hid them: plan 25's leftovers, wave 76). On it: on the left a disc 92 across, white rimmed in the Dynamax red, with the Mega Max mark (the Key Stone in the red ring); beside it the trainer's class and name in capitals (ExtraBold 24, Muted, "CHAMPION CYNTHIA") over the ace's name with its gender (Black 40) and its status; its level at the right end; under them a bar 30 tall across the box (the HP bar's colours, the punch's white ghost and the box's jolt as any box's, plan 25 · YD2), holding exactly the share the box would (`CombatantView.DisplayedHp` over the most, the raised numbers of `TakeRaisedHp`); and at the bar's right end, on its row, the three turns as three pips 13 in radius, 34 apart, lit Dynamax red (rimmed in its dark) for each turn left and an empty ring for each spent, the one going fading out as the pill's number falls (`TickOf`). The Dynamax pill is not hung on it (the pips are the turns). The trainer's tray of balls hangs under it as under the box. When the giant falls the box narrows back over the fall (its share eased back with the end's shrink) and is the foe's own box again.
- **A Tera Type on the HP box**: once a Pokémon has Terastallized a crown of three facets in its Tera Type's colour (Stellar in four colours) 34 wide hangs on the box's top edge beside the Dynamax pill's place (its right edge 150 in from the box's), on a white disc rimmed in Frame, for the rest of the battle.
- **AUTO** (plan 12 · Q12, the options' AUTO-BATTLE row, off unless chosen; `ModernUi.AutoButton`): where the row is on and the battle lets the player choose (not the Great Marsh, Pal Park, the catching lesson, the Battle Palace or a battle watched), the commands carry a fifth button, 176 × 52 (radius 26), hung on the message box's top edge at its right end: its right edge 24 in from the box's (at 1084), its top 30 above the box (828), so it sits over the box's corner and clear of the player's HP box above. Teal (`40,160,168`) with the kit's button gradient and AUTO in white Black 26; the cursor on it is the kit's white ring and glow. Chosen, it plays the turn for the player and goes on turn after turn: each Pokémon uses the move `BattleEngine.Suggest` names, aimed at the first foe, and each line is read on after 0.9 s (sooner where the auto-advance is quicker). While it runs the button stays where it stood, lit Gold with AUTO in Ink, over every line and menu (on a line's wider box it stands a little right of the box's middle, clear of a level's panel at the box's right end), and a hint pill at the top right (64 in, 44 down) says how to stop it (Esc Stop auto). It stops by itself on a faint on the player's side, a move to learn, Battle Style Shift's offer and the battle's end, and Back stops it at any line; then the button is plain again. It never opens the bag, so it never throws a ball. With the row off nothing is drawn and the commands stand as they always did.
- **Cursors** (`BattleEngine.MoveCursor`): a cursor moves the way its buttons lie. Among the commands, up and down go through FIGHT, BAG, POKÉMON and RUN in that order and round again; left and right cross between FIGHT and the stack beside it, and come back into the stack on the row the cursor stood on last. Where AUTO is offered (plan 12 · Q12) left from FIGHT goes to it and right from it back to FIGHT; up and down leave it where it is. The move menu has no cursor on an empty place, and opens on the move that Pokémon chose last in this battle. Among a double battle's targets the cursor moves over the four cards as they lie and never stops on one that can't be aimed at: a step up or down that would land on one takes the card beside it. Enter answers a battle's menu, as it reads a battle's message on.
- **The Palace's closed menu** (plan 14 · B9, `ModernUi.PalaceClosed`): in a Battle Palace battle the commands never open, since every Pokémon chooses by its nature. While any line is read a plate stands at the top right in their stead (`PalacePlate`, 1340, 52, 524 × 184, radius 26, the menus' panel): CHOOSING BY NATURE (Black 28, Ink) and under it why (Bold 22, Muted), then the four commands as pills 226 × 34, two to a row 238 apart, in the bag-shut grey (`Closed`) with their words in white Black 20. Each turn opens on the battle's own line for each of the player's Pokémon, "X is deciding for itself what to do...", which is the moment the plate is looked at. It stands clear of the foe's box (top left) and the player's (bottom right), and nothing on it can be chosen.
- **The judge's card** (plan 14 · B9, `ModernUi.JudgeCard`): when a bout of the Battle Arena is judged, a panel 840 × 470 (radius 30) stands over the middle of the stage (`JudgeRect`, from 540, 150) for as long as the verdict's line is read: JUDGMENT in Red Black 40 centred at its head, the two Pokémon's names (Black 28) over two columns 320 apart, the player's in Blue on the left and the foe's in Red on the right, then MIND, SKILL and BODY (Black 30, Ink) on rows 76 apart, each column with its mark: a Green ring (radius 24, 7 wide) for the count won, a Gold triangle for one level, a Red cross (two strokes 8 wide) for one lost. Under a Rule line, TOTAL and each side's points (Black 40), the winner's in Gold. Our own face for Emerald's judgment window; the line after it names who takes the bout.

### Summary
