One Pokémon on three panels: on the left its number, level, the 128-px sprite at 3× on a pale disc, name, category, type pills (with its status and a PKRS tag after them), nature and experience (points, to next level, bar); top right the six stats, each with its value and a bar whose length and colour follow the species' base stat (red under 50, amber under 80, green under 110, teal above; HP shows current / max in the HP colours); bottom right the moves as rows with type pill, name, category, power, accuracy and PP. Up and down step through the team. **A Pokémon held** (plan 18 · W4b-3): where the player's Pokémon are held to a level (a run's cap, or a crossed region's ceiling) and this one stands at it or over it, the experience's last row (the caption, the points to the next level and the bar) moves 14 up and a line under the bar says so, "Gains no EXP above Lv. N for now.", in ExtraBold 22 Muted; one that isn't held (and a rental) shows nothing more, where it always stood. The team's summary and the battle's say it; the PC's doesn't, since a boxed Pokémon gains nothing.

**Pages** (plan 08 · P3, `SummaryView`: its page, its pages and its cursors, with nothing drawn): INFO is the first (the left panel and, on the right, the held item with what it does above a panel of the six marks), then the memo, SKILLS (the stats as six rows 66 apart with the ability and its text under a rule), the two pages of moves, the condition once the Contest Hall has been visited, and the ribbons last. **The move pages** (plan 08 · P4, `MovesPanel`, `MoveTextPanel`): a panel of the four moves as rows 98 apart (type pill, name, category; then power, accuracy and PP on BATTLE MOVES, or the move's contest quality and the three hearts it earns by itself, filled in red, on CONTEST MOVES) and under it a panel 400 high with the move chosen: its name in Black 44 and either its type pill, category and description, or what its effect does on the contest stage. The confirm button brings a cursor up on the first move (a Selection rim and a pale fill on the row), up and down move it, either button puts it away; with no cursor the first move's text is shown. **The same view is opened from three places** (`SummaryView.Press` reads the keys alike in each): the team's menu, the PC's box menu (SUMMARY, between WITHDRAW or STORE and MARK; up and down go through the team or the places of the box that hold a Pokémon) and the battle's switching cards (the item button, C, with its hint beside the cards' own; up and down go on through the team and the cards' cursor follows; no marks to set and no condition page there). The confirm button on INFO brings a cursor up on the marks (a rounded square, a Selection rim on a pale fill, round the mark): left and right move it, the button sets or clears the mark, cancel puts it away; a set mark is Ink, a clear one Rule. An Egg has no marks, and a single-page summary (a rental's) keeps the stats above its moves.

**Effort on SKILLS** (plan 36 · TR1, `ModernUi.StatRows` with `effort`): each of the six stat rows gains, under its base-stat bar, a thin second bar for that stat's effort (the same track, 8 tall, its middle 18 below the row's middle), measured against the cap the rules in force give a stat (252 under the modern rules, Platinum's 255): Selection while it is under the cap and Gold once it reaches it, with `n / 252` in Black 20, Muted, right-aligned in the value's column under the stat's value. Under the six rows, 60 below the last row's middle, EFFORT in the Label style, the total out of 510 in Black 26 (Ink) and a thin bar across the track's width (Selection, Gold at 510); the rule and ABILITY under it keep their places. A stat brought to its peak (hyper training, plan 36 · TR3) shows a small PEAK tag, a Gold pill 70 × 26 with white Black 18 text, at the right end of its row's effort bar, and the same pill stands on that stat's row of the PC's judge (`ModernUi.Teams.cs`), 12 left of the word's pill and centred on its row; on SKILLS the tag's top is 12 under the row's middle, just clear of the base-stat bar (`ModernUi.PeakTag`, drawn since TR3). The rental summary and the one-page summary draw no effort, and the base-stat bar stays what it was whatever the effort.

**The Tera Type on INFO** (plan 36 · TR5, `ModernUi.TeraPill`): while the bag holds a Tera Orb, the held item's panel shows TERA TYPE in the Label style at its top right, 44 in from the panel's edge, and under it, right-aligned, the Pokémon's Tera Type as a gem and a pill, 42 high: the gem a diamond 0.84 of the height tall and 0.67 wide, its upper half the type's colour lightened by a quarter and its lower half darkened by 0.15, then 0.95 of the height on from the gem's left the type's own pill (`TypePill`). Stellar's gem is white over a pale steel lower half, and its pill five bands across (red 226 92 92, amber 226 170 60, green 80 176 104, blue 70 136 220, violet 150 96 206) with STELLAR in white Black. Without a Tera Orb nothing is drawn and the panel is as it was. The team's summary and the PC's show it; a rental's and a battle's don't.

**Marks and titles** (plan 10 · F9, `ModernUi.Marks.cs`): a mark of the moment a Pokémon was met is our own badge, never the games' pictures: a disc in the colour of its kind (rare gold 232 176 40, a personality violet 150 96 206, a time of day blue 70 120 222, a weather teal 28 156 164, the rod sea green 40 140 110, an alpha red 214 64 64) with a darker rim and two short tails under it, a white ring inside, and a white star in its middle for the rare mark or a white dot for any other (`MarkBadge`). On INFO, a Pokémon met with a mark has its six markings raised to the panel's upper half and, under a rule, MET WITH and each of its marks as a cell (the badge at radius 30, the mark's name in Black 30, and a Gold TITLE tag after the one chosen), then TITLE and the line it is sent out under ("Sent out as Starly the Sleepy") or, in Muted, that none is chosen. The marks' cursor goes on from the six markings into these cells (a Selection rim and pale fill round the cell); the button there makes the mark the title, or takes it away when it is the title already. A Pokémon met with none shows the six markings where they always stood. On its party card (at its upper right, radius 20) and beside its sprite in the PC's detail panel (radius 22) a Pokémon shows the badge of its title, or of its first mark when none is chosen; nothing for one met with none, nor for an Egg.

**The trainer memo** (plan 08 · P3, `ModernUi.MemoPanel`) is the page after the first: left and right turn to it, the left side staying as it is. One panel as tall as the two: the trainer's name (Black 40) and ID No. (five digits) at the top, a rule, MET ON with the day (Black 34) and under it, in ExtraBold 30, where and at what level the Pokémon was met ("Hatched at…" for a level of nought, a plain "met somewhere" when the place is unknown) and its nature; a rule, CHARACTERISTIC with its line in Black 34 (`Characteristics`: the stat of the highest IV, five lines to a stat, our own words); a rule, ABILITY with its name in Black 36 and its description in Bold 30 Muted. A Pokémon without a trainer's mark is the player's own and shows the player's name and number.

Once the Contest Hall has been visited (plan 06 · R14c), left and right turn the right-hand side to a second page and back: the Pokémon's condition (below, "Poffins") on one panel as tall as the two, its chart in the middle and the sheen at its foot. Before then that page isn't there, as in Platinum. The last page is the Pokémon's ribbons (plan 06 · R17b; below, "Ribbons"), always there: the same panel with the ribbons as cards, and the button puts a cursor on them.

### The stage show

Plan 42 · PS1, PS2 (`UI/ModernUi.StageShow.cs`; the props worn on the model are "Props worn" under "Pokémon (version 2, G7)"). Every piece of it is ours: no stage, prop, picture or performer of any game.

**The moods' colours** (`ModernUi.MoodColour`): bright 242 190 60, dashing 200 52 62, gentle 120 196 172, eerie 132 92 196. A prop's chip is a disc of its mood's colour with its own body colour inside it, a third smaller, and its name after it in ExtraBold.

**The dressing room** (`DressingRoomScreen`): a backdrop from plum 64 36 54 to 28 16 30, a row of fourteen warm bulbs (255 226 150, radius 11, 62 apart) across the top as round a mirror, the title DRESSING ROOM. First a panel at the left of the team to choose from (no Egg), a row of 76 each; the Pokémon under the cursor turns on the dais meanwhile. Then three things: the slots down the left (a panel 440 wide, a row of 108 to each slot the body offers, its name in Black 22 Muted and the piece worn as a chip, or "(nothing)"), the Pokémon in the middle on a dais (a rounded plinth of 196 160 110 to 120 90 58 at y 750, 520 wide) turning a revolution every eight seconds in the gallery's turntable view, 620 across, wearing what is on it, and at the right the trunk's pieces for the slot under the cursor (a panel 520 wide, seven rows of 76, "Take it off" first, each piece's count after it), with the moods worn under it: the four moods' discs, radius 26, the count of pieces of each in white Black 32. The slot or piece under the cursor is Gold lightened by 0.4; a slot whose pieces are open is lightened by 0.7.

**The stage** (`StageShowScreen`): a back wall from 30 22 44 to 14 10 24; boards from y 700 to 920 (132 86 52 to 86 54 32, a seam every 160); six folds of curtain at each side (140 28 44 and 84 14 28 by turns, 60 wide, a fold every 36) and a pelmet across the top; 24 footlights (255 226 150, radius 9) along y 912. The four performers stand 420 apart from x 330, each drawn 400 across with their feet at y 790, rendered each frame in their own pictures; whoever has the spotlight stands in a cone of warm light (255 236 170 at alpha 46) from the top to a pool at their feet. Over each head a bar of the crowd's eyes (300 wide, the player's Gold, the cast's Blue lightened by 0.2) with its share and the performer's name under it. The crowd's heads in silhouette (10 8 18) along the foot, bobbing while a cue is on. The beat along the foot: a disc for every beat of the bar, the one under way larger and lit (Gold on the player's cue, white otherwise), and YOUR CUE! over it in Gold Black 34 while the spotlight is the player's. The curtain rises over 1.6 s with the show's name on it in the display face, 84. The results: a panel 960 wide, THE CROWD'S VERDICT, the four by place in rows of 100, the player's in Gold lightened by 0.4, each with its share of the eyes and its score.

### The film studio

Plan 42 · PS4 (`Battle/BattleEngine.Studio.cs`). A film is shot on the battle's own screen, with nothing drawn of its own in part a: everything it says is a line of the battle's message box, and a line to choose is the box's question.

- **The title card**: the take's first line, before any beat: the film's title, its genre and the take's number, then "Lights, camera... action!", in the box with no speaker's pill.
- **A beat's line**: in the box with its speaker's name on the pill, as a trainer's own line (plan 11 · C12): the co-star's, the foe's, or the player's own name for a line the player says. Two rows at most; nothing steps onto a platform, since nobody of the film stands on one.
- **A line to choose**: the beat's last line stays in the box and its two or three lines stand beside it where Yes and No do (`DrawChoices`, the cursor on the first), each short enough for one row of the choices' panel. The line chosen is then said in the box under the player's name.
- **The stars**: the ending's lines under their speaker's pill, then "That take earned N out of 5 stars!" with no pill, the take's last line.
- **The premiere** (part b): the best take watched as a recorded battle is (the lines standing their time and going on by themselves), the take's chosen lines said again under the player's name, then its ending, its stars, the crowd's line (cheers, applause or murmurs, no pill) and "Box office: N tickets sold!" (the number grouped with commas in every language), the premiere's last line.
- **The lot** is Black 2's place as imported, its people and words ours; its music is the studio's own march (`unova/pokestar_studios`, F major, brass over a striding bass, a strings bridge in D minor).
- **The posters** (the user's 33a: painted, theatrical, in warm reds; `PropType.PosterWall`, `Landmarks.PaintPosterWall`): a hoarding of dark wood 92 by 60 texels on three posts, standing beside the studio building's door, with the studio's four films painted on it side by side, each poster 18 by 39: a crimson ground (140 26 34 at its foot) warming in bands of three rows to a lit orange (232 108 64) at its top, as a stage is lit from above, a pale ring of the spotlight (250 168 104) over its emblem, the emblem in cream (244 228 196), and a gold band (226 176 72) at its foot with three dark strokes for its title. The four emblems, left to right: an empty theatre seat (The Empty Seat), a clock with a gold rim at a minute to the hour (The Gilded Hour), a letter with a green seal (Letters in the Fog), a gold bolt over two dark peaks (Thunder at Marlowe Pass). It stands on the three tiles before the building's front east of its door (22 to 24, 21), blocks them, and is read from the tile before it, naming the films whose takes the studio has kept.

### Sports days

Plan 42 · PS5 (`Models/SportsDays.cs`; rulings, "Sports days"). Nimbasa City's five fields are Black 2's places as imported (`--game black2 --data`), drawn as every room of Unova is; nothing of their own is drawn but what the language already draws.

- **The day's field**: between games the receptionist of each stadium takes the player down onto the field of the day's sport through a fade (Big Stadium: baseball, football, American football; Small Court: tennis, basketball). On it stand the sport's three athletes in their kit (Black 2's looks), each battled once a day, and the backers who cheer for them.
- **A game on** (the twilight hours, 17:00 to 19:59): the lobby's three policemen stand across the way to the field, the stands' crowd fills the seats, and coming up to the stands plays the game's scene from the rail: the cinema bars slide in (0.4 s), the camera travels slowly over the rail (2 tiles a second) and back, the crowd's cheer is heard (`contest_cheer`) and the scene's lines (no speaker's pill: the stadium's announcer is the game's own voice) tell the play and the final whistle; the bars slide away and the camera comes home. A crowd's bed under it is owed (the ambience has no crowd yet).

### The photo tour

Plan 44 · SD5 (`Graphics/CourseScene.cs`, `CourseRenderer.cs`, `UI/PhotoTourScreen.cs`, `ModernUi.PhotoTour.cs`). A course is a scene of its own in the battle's smooth style, never a map of a region: one 3D scene a frame, drawn from a `TourFrame` (the camera, every resident's look, action, pose time, place and facing, the fruit, the hour and the weather), so a ride's live picture and a kept photograph are the same drawing.

- **The course**: a strip of ground along its rail in the arena kit's pieces (the meadow's ground, `SoftTextures.Water` for its water, soft trees, rocks and tufts, hills on the horizon), lit by the battle's rig for its kind of ground at the ride's hour (`ArtLook.ArenaRig`) and laid over by the ride's weather (`ArtLook.InBattleWeather`). The Reed River is a slow river 8 wide between two meadow banks, reeds (tall tufts) at the water's edge, round trees beyond, the rail on the east bank at 1.6 above the ground.
- **The residents** are the battle's models, posed by `PokemonAnimation`: idle; a walk's stride; asleep (eyes shut and drooping, `Sleepy`); eating (`Eating`, head down); playing (a cheer's bounce toward the other); showing off a move (`Attack` of its kind); a reaction (the cheer, facing the lens); the rare action (the cheer with a hop of a third of its height). Each stands at its height in tiles by its species' metres (0.35 + 0.8 × metres, 0.4 to 2.4), the same height the judge measures.
- **The viewfinder**: the picture whole, four corner brackets 64 long and 6 thick in white at 80% (inset 96 by 64), a cross of two 24-long strokes at the middle; at the foot a bar of the rail's way (600 wide, Gold over Panel), the film left (a roll's icon and "FILM 47") and the fruit left at the left, the zoom (×1.0 to ×3.0, five ticks) at the right. A shot is a white flash over the picture (alpha from 200 to nothing over 0.18 s) and the film's count turning; a thrown fruit is a red-gold sphere of 0.18 on its arc; a whistle is three white rings widening from the middle of the picture over 0.6 s.
- **The opening and the end**: the ride opens from black over 0.8 s once every resident's model is made (never by a frame first); at the rail's end, or with the film gone, the picture closes to black and the judged panel comes up: the ride's best photograph of each species, a row of 84 each (the species' icon, its name, its stars in Gold, its points right-aligned), eight rows at once with the menu screens' cursor, window and scroll bar past eight (plan 44 · SD6), the ride's total under it in Black 40, and KEEP (the item button) to put the best of them in the photo book.
- **The six courses** (plan 44 · SD6), each lit by the battle's rig for its kind of ground at the ride's hour, the rail on the east side at 1.6 (2.2 on the High Pass), the residents west of it, and each course's screens drawn as its own thing at their places and sizes:
  - *Reed River* (a meadow by a lake): as above.
  - *Deep Wood* (the forest's rig): the forest floor, tall round soft trees in rows on both sides (the near side's thinned so the lens sees between them), ferns, fallen logs and mushrooms on the floor; its screens are round bushes.
  - *Reef Shore* (the sea's rig): a beach of pale sand the rail runs along, the sea west of x −1.5 whitening to foam at the shore, clumps of coral fingers in warm colours in the shallows, dunes behind the beach and islands far out; its screens are rocks.
  - *Cavern* (the cave's rig, which ignores the hour): the cave's floor between walls of lumpy rock both sides and closing ahead, stalagmites, blue and violet crystals glowing; its screens are rocks.
  - *High Pass* (the snow's rig): a stony road with drifts in its hollows, snowy pines up both sides, white peaks all round; its screens are boulders capped with snow. Snow is a ride's third weather here.
  - *Night Garden* (the meadow's rig after dark: the course is ridden at 21:00 when begun by day): lawns, a pale gravel path under the rail and across the lawns, long hedges both sides, clipped hedges for screens, flowerbeds of small blooms in four colours, lamps along the path (a dark post, a warm lantern with its glow and a pool of light on the ground), round trees beyond.
- **The report** (plan 44 · SD6; `UI/TourReportScreen.cs`, `ModernUi.TourReport.cs`): the menu screens' frame titled TOUR REPORT. Six tabs, a course each in its own colour (Reed River meadow green 76 170 96, Deep Wood deep green 46 120 74, Reef Shore sea blue 60 150 214, Cavern stone brown 128 112 100, High Pass slate blue 132 156 196, Night Garden dusk violet 124 92 196), a star on its chip once open and a key while shut. Under them on the left a panel 820 wide of the course's species, a list row each (its name, its stars in Gold from x 380, its points right-aligned), seven at once, scrolling; a shut course's panel says the total that opens it. On the right **the print**: the chosen photograph redrawn from its record (its course's scenery from its camera, every resident at its pose), 16:9, on a cream mount 16 wide (250 248 240); "Developing..." on a dark card while its models are being made, never waited for. Under the print the species' name in Black 40 with its stars at the right, a four-star photograph's rare action in Gold (`photoTour.rare.*`), its points. At the foot the report's total in Black 36, the course's in Bold 28 beside it, and the guide's word for the total in Gold under them (six bands, from 0, 5,000, 15,000, 30,000, 50,000 and 75,000 points).

### Menu screens (G10)

Every full-screen menu is the same frame: the backdrop, its title at the top left, key hints at the top right, and its content between rows 132 and 1032 with 64 margins and 32 gutters. Its parts rise or slide into place over 0.25–0.45 s when it opens.

Shared parts (`ModernUi`, in `ModernUi.Lists.cs`):

- **List rows** (`ListRow`): 84 tall and 8 apart, radius 26, on a panel. What the row is about sits at its left on a pale disc (an item's icon at 3×, a Pokémon's at 1×), its name in Black 34, and one value right-aligned (a count "×5", a price). The chosen row is a Selection-coloured pill with white text, as in the start menu; the others have no fill. A row that can't be chosen now is Muted.
- **Scrolling** (`UiNav.Window`, `ScrollBar`): a list shows whole rows only. The cursor keeps two rows of context ahead of it where there are any, and wraps from the last row to the first. A thin bar at the panel's right edge (Rule-coloured track, Frame-coloured thumb) shows where the window is, and only when there is more than fits.
- **A key held down** (`HeldKey`): in a list too long to tap through, a press is one step and a key kept down goes on: after 0.32 s a step every 0.08 s, and from the tenth of those one every 0.04 s. Held steps stop at either end of the list, where a press wraps round, and the cursor's tick sounds at most every 0.07 s (`CursorTick`). The Pokédex's list and entries, a pocket's list in the bag and a shop's stock do this; short menus on those screens (an item's actions, "how many?", the party) take presses only.
- **Tabs** (`Tabs`): pills in a row across the screen under the title, each with its sign on a chip in its own colour and its name in Black 22. The chosen tab is filled with its colour, its chip white. Left and right change tab and wrap round.
- **Detail panel**: the thing chosen, large, on a pale disc (an item's icon at 6×, a Pokémon's sprite at 3×), its name in Black 44–56, tags, a rule, and its text in Bold 30 on 42 leading.
- **Money** (`Money`): our own mark, a P crossed by two bars, before the number in Black. Amounts on panels and in lists use it, never "$" or "¥".
- **Stepper** (`Stepper`): a number on a Frame-coloured pill between two arrows; left and right change it by one, up and down by ten, and it wraps between the least and the most.
- **Keyboard** (`Keyboard`, its cursor and rules in `NameEntry`): keys as small cards, 96 by 84 and 10 apart, in four rows of ten: the alphabet with a full stop, a hyphen, an apostrophe and a space, then the digits. The chosen key is a Selection-coloured pill. A fifth row has three wide keys: upper or lower case, delete, and OK. The keys turn to lower case by themselves after a name's first capital; the B button deletes, the Start button jumps to OK, and the cursor waits on OK once the name is full. Names are entered with the cursor and never typed, because the game's own keys are letters. **A language's own letters** (plan 17 · N5, the Romanian letters): a language whose names need letters the board lacks lists them in `languages.json` as `keyboard`, in capitals (Romanian: `ĂÂÎȘȚ`). The board then has a **fifth row of letters** under the digits, one key for each, left-aligned on the first columns of the grid (the rest of the row is empty, with no key drawn), and the wide keys move down to a sixth row; the board is 94 taller and still fits its panel. The row follows the case key like the others (`ăâîșț` in lower case); the cursor wraps within its own keys, and a step up or down into it from a column past its end lands on its last key. A language with no `keyboard` gets the board of four rows exactly as before: English's pictures do not move. The row is fixed when the keyboard opens, by the language then chosen. Names keep their length rules (seven characters, the box's eight); a name is saved as UTF-8 text like any other, and every key is a character both faces can draw (the comma-below Ș ș Ț ț are in the second face).

The screens:

- **Bag** (`BagScreen`): eight tabs, Platinum's pockets in its order (Items, Medicine, Poké Balls, TMs & HMs, Berries, Mail, Battle Items, Key Items), each in a colour of its own (gold, pink, red, violet, green, sky blue, orange, purple) and each remembering its own cursor. Under them the pocket's list on the left (eight rows) and the chosen item on the right: icon, name, its pocket as a tag, how many are in the bag, and its text; a TM or HM is described by its move, with the move's type, category, power, accuracy and PP under it. The A button opens what can be done with the item on a small panel at the foot of the detail panel (USE, GIVE, TOSS, REGISTER, CANCEL as the item allows, one row each, like the start menu's rows). Using or giving goes on to the party cards, which say ABLE or NOT ABLE for something used (a TM's tag is ABLE, NOT ABLE or LEARNED; ABLE is green). An item used on one move (an Ether, a PP Up) then shows the evolution scene's move panel on the right (`MovePick`: the Pokémon's four moves with their PP, the question as its title), and a TM taught to a Pokémon that knows four moves asks which to forget on the same panel. TOSS asks how many on a panel over the dimmed screen like the shop's: the item's icon and name, a stepper from 1 to as many as the bag holds. **Sorted** (plan 12 · Q13, behind the same BOXES ANYWHERE row): a Gold tag 38 high at the top right of the detail panel, names the order the pocket is kept in (ORDER: AS TAKEN, A TO Z or MOST FIRST; a TM pocket by name is by number); the item button (C) turns it to the next order and plays the page sound, and Q and E (the shoulders) jump the cursor to the first item of the next or the previous initial letter in A TO Z (the pocket's tab is then turned by Left and Right alone). Each pocket remembers its order, and the save keeps them.
- **Item icons** (`PixelArtGenerator.ItemIcon`): 20 by 20 pixel art, shown at 3× in rows and 6× on the detail disc. A kind has a shape: the ball itself, a spray bottle for medicine (its colour says which), a crystal for a Revive, a wrapped sweet for a Rare Candy, a flat disc in its move's type colour for a TM (a pale rim for an HM), a berry, an envelope, a key, a tonic bottle, a cut gem in its own colour for an evolution stone, a pouch for anything else.
- **Shop** (`ShopScreen`): the counter opens on its question, BUY, SELL and SEE YA!, three rows on a panel in the middle of the dimmed screen. Buying shows the stock on the left (nine rows, each with its price; a price the player can't pay is Red), the player's money on a card at the top right, the chosen item under it with how many are already in the bag. Selling shows the bag's items that sell in the same rows, each with what one fetches (half the price) and how many are held. Either asks how many on a panel over the dimmed screen: the item, a stepper from 1 to as many as the money buys or the bag holds (99 at most), and the total.
- **Wardrobe** (`WardrobeScreen`, plan 11 · C10): the player's clothes at home and a boutique's counter are one screen, titled WARDROBE or with the boutique's place. Five tabs, the slots (HATS red, TOPS blue, BOTTOMS violet, SHOES orange, BAGS green), each with its own sign. On the left a stage 520 wide with the player's figure on a pale floor disc, rendered live in what the cursor is on (three dots pulse at its top while a new outfit is being made); on the BAGS tab the figure turns half away so the bag shows. In the middle the slot's clothes as list rows (eight to the panel): the look's own first, its disc a gold star; then the free, the owned and those for sale, each disc the garment's colour ringed with its second, a crossed ring for none at all. A row ends in a WORN tag (green) for what is on, OWNED for what has been bought, or the price (Red if the money doesn't reach). On the right the money on a card and the garment's own card: its name in Black 40, its slot's tag and WORN or OWNED, the price, a rule and its line. Choosing something owned puts it on at once; something for sale asks "Buy the …?" on a panel over the dimmed screen with BUY (green) and NO (blue), and a garment bought is worn at once and owned for good.
  - **The salon and the dye house** (plan 43 · LF3; `SalonScreen`, `ModernUi.Salon.cs`): the wardrobe's frame on a screen of their own, titled SALON or DYE HOUSE. The salon's four tabs are CUT (red, the hat's sign), COLOUR (violet, a star), EYES (blue, a disc) and SKIN (orange, the trainer's sign); the dye house's two are its steps, GARMENT (blue) and DYE (red). The figure on the left wears what the cursor is on; the list's first row is the look's own (a gold star on its disc), each colour a swatch on its disc, WORN in Green on what is worn; on the right the money and a card with the price, for a dye AND A BERRY with the berry's name, and the counter's note. A change asks a question over the dimmed screen as buying does (the thing's name, its price, YES and NO). The stylist stands at the boutique's west wall and the dyer at its east (Jubilife), the dyer by Lumiose's counter; decision 1's house of its own for the salon is owed.
- **Poffins** (plan 06 · R14c; `ModernUi.Poffins.cs`). Our own pictures, never the games': a Poffin is a round puff, the pot an iron ring seen from above.
  - **The flavours' colours**: spicy red (230 86 74), dry blue (74 132 228), sweet pink (238 118 172), bitter green (76 174 96), sour yellow (232 190 50). The condition's qualities take the colours of the flavours that raise them (cool red, beauty blue, cute pink, smart green, tough yellow).
  - **A Poffin** (`PoffinIcon`): a base a little wider than tall in its strongest flavour's colour, a domed top over it in its second flavour's (a lighter shade of the first for one flavour), a swirl and a shine on the dome. A Rich Poffin is caramel brown, an Overripe one plum, a Mild one cream, and a Foul one grey with a wisp of smoke rising from it.
  - **The pot** (`PoffinCookingScreen`, titled POFFIN COOKING): in the middle of the screen a cream table 940 wide, ringed with small red dots, and on it the pot: an iron ring (radius 340) over flames that peek out under its rim, more and taller in each of the three stages. In it the batter, the colour of its berries' flavours mixed (each as strong as the flavour) and a little cream (42 %): glossy and runny in the first stage, thicker in the second, a dough in the third. Pale swirls on it turn as the pot says the batter turns. A wooden spoon stands where the stylus is, its handle out over the rim. A gold arrow curves round the pot's top the way to stir: large and pulsing when the pot draws it again, smaller afterwards, red while the batter goes the other way. The berry falls in from above and leaves a ripple. Burning shows as grey spots on the batter and smoke, spilling as batter slopping over the rim.
  - **Beside the pot**: on the left a panel with the stages (three numbered dots: the current one gold, those done green), the time stirred, and how often it burned and spilled over; on the right a tall gauge of the batter's speed, orange at its foot where it burns, green in the middle, blue at the top where it spills (FAST in the last stage, where nothing spills), with a white bar for the speed now. A banner over the pot's foot says what happens: the berry going in, "Done!", and the warnings in Red.
  - **The results**: a card over the dimmed pot with the time, the spills, the burns and the smoothness as captions over rules on its left, and a second later the Poffin on a disc on its right, with its name and level. The text box then says it was put away and asks whether to cook another (YES, NO).
  - **The Poffin Case** (`PoffinCaseScreen`): six tabs, the five flavours in their colours and then ALL (slate); a Poffin is under every flavour it has. The list on the left, seven rows (the Poffin on the row's disc, its name, its level), newest first; under it the prompt on a panel 80 tall. On the right the chosen Poffin: how many the case holds of its hundred on a tag (Red when full), the Poffin at large on a disc, its name, its level on a tag of its colour and its smoothness on a Frame-coloured one, and under a rule its five flavours as bars in their colours, each with the quality it raises; a flavour it hasn't is Muted. The A button opens GIVE, TRASH and CANCEL like the bag's actions; TRASH asks over the dimmed screen, YES in Red and NO in Blue.
  - **Feeding**: GIVE goes to the party's cards; then the Pokémon's card on the left (its sprite at 3× on a disc, name, level and nature) over the Poffin's card, and its condition on the right. Given the Poffin, the Pokémon hops, the old shape stays as a white outline and the new one grows out of it over 0.8 s, and the prompt says how it took it (a flavour it likes, one it dislikes, or neither).
  - **The condition** (`ConditionChart`): a pentagon on a pale disc, cool straight up and then with the clock beauty, cute, smart and tough, each named on a pill of its colour at its point; three pentagon rules and a spoke to each point. The Pokémon's shape is filled in green (72 200 112) with a darker edge: nothing in a quality is a small pentagon at a seventh of the radius, 255 the rim, in a straight line between. Under it the sheen: twelve stars in a row, as many lit (gold, with a white heart) as the sheen gives (twelve at 255), twinkling in turn; the rest small and in the Rule colour.
- **Super Contests** (plan 06 · R17a; `SuperContestScreen`, `ModernUi.SuperContest.cs`). One screen through a whole contest, titled SUPER CONTEST with two tags beside the title: the rank in Frame (PRACTICE for a round held alone) and the quality in its colour (the condition's: cool red, beauty blue, cute pink, smart green, tough yellow). At the foot of every phase the announcer's box, a panel 148 tall across the screen with one or two lines at 34 and the advance arrow when a button moves it on. Our own pictures: nothing of the original's stage, judges or accessories is drawn.
  - **A contestant** is their Pokémon, as a menu sprite or a menu icon, never a trainer's figure. The four stand in the order of their entry numbers from the left, No. 1 to No. 4; the player is No. 4, and the player's tag is Selection where the others' are Frame.
  - **The line-up**: four cards across (the entry tag, the sprite at 2× on a Poké Ball disc, the Pokémon's name, the trainer's under it in Muted). The one being announced has the quality's glow and hops. The theme is then given on a band across the dimmed cards, framed in the quality's colour: THE THEME, its name at 76, and a tag with how many accessories and seconds there are.
  - **The dressing-up**: on the left a panel 700 wide with the theme's name, a tag of how many accessories are on of how many the rank allows (Red when full), the Pokémon at 3× on a disc with what it wears round it, and the clock: the seconds as a number over a bar that runs down, Green and then Red for the last ten. On the right the Fashion Case: a grid six across of cards, each an accessory's sign with how many are left in the case (a grey disc when none is), the cursor's card in Selection, five rows in view; under it the name of what the cursor is on.
  - **An accessory's sign** (`AccessoryIcon`): one of nine simple shapes by what the thing is (a fluff as three puffs, a pebble as a rounded block, a feather, leaf or scale as a slanted capsule with a rib, a moustache or barrette as a bow, a flower or sparkle as five petals round a middle, a claw, flame or drop as a teardrop, a mushroom, parasol or balloon as a head on a stick, a hat as a crown on a brim, a stage as a block on a wider foot), in the colour its name gives (Red Feather, Blue Scale) or its kind's own (a pebble grey, a coin gold), with a darker rim and a light spot. They are signs, not drawings of the accessories: 100 would be a sheet of art of their own (plan 09).
  - **A look**: the contestant on the left as in the dressing-up (an opponent's accessories stand where its dressing puts them), and on the right its name, its trainer, the theme on a tag, and the hearts: CONDITION a row of eight, DRESSING UP a row of four, the hearts won filled in heart red (236 84 110) one after another, six a second, each a little larger as it comes; the rest in Rule. Under them the total as a number at 84 beside one heart.
  - **The dance**: across the top the bar as a dark track (30 34 58), its second half a shade lighter; the beats are thin rules, every fourth taller; a hand in Selection crosses it once a bar. The lead's steps are white arrows where they fell, and each has a ring half a bar on with a pale arrow in it: where it is to be repeated. The player's own steps leave a dot under the track, Gold for an excellent one, Green for a good one, Muted for a miss. Under the bar the four Pokémon on discs, the lead's larger and in a light shade of the quality's colour with a LEAD tag; a step lifts the dancer for 0.3 s and shows its arrow on a disc of the grade's colour beside it; each has its points under its name. The four arrow keys are the four steps.
  - **The acting**: three judges' cards across the top left (the name, a HEAD tag in Gold on the middle one, five stars of voltage lit in Gold, and under them the menu icons of whoever has performed for them this turn); under them the four contestants as rows in the turn's order (the icon on a disc, name and trainer, the move shown with its quality's tag and "for" its judge, the turn's hearts as +N beside a heart, the total at the right); on the right the player's four moves as cards: the name, the quality's tag, the hearts it is worth, VOLTAGE UP in Green or VOLTAGE DOWN in Red where it moves the voltage in this contest, and its effect in a line; a move shown the turn before is Muted and says so. The card being chosen has the Selection border; while a turn plays, whoever is acting and the judge spoken of have the quality's glow, and each thing that happens is a line in the announcer's box that a button or 2.4 s moves on.
  - **The results**: four rows on one panel (the entry tag, the icon, name and trainer) each with a track on which the three rounds' bars follow one another, VISUAL pink (238 118 172), DANCE blue (74 132 228), ACTING gold (240 176 56), each growing over 1.4 s as its round is announced; a third of the track is a round's full length. Then the places at the right of the tracks, the winner's row framed in Gold and its icon hopping. A new ribbon is a rosette in the quality's colour (`Rosette`: twelve loops round a gold middle with a white star, two tails) in the announcer's box beside the line that gives it. A practice round shows one bar a contestant, in its round's colour, with the score.
- **Ball Capsules and Seals** (plan 06 · R17b; `SealCaseScreen`, `SealShopScreen`, `ModernUi.Capsules.cs`, `Graphics/SealFx.cs`). Our own pictures: a Seal is a sticker and a capsule a pale ball, never the games' sprites.
  - **A Seal's sticker** (`SealSticker`): a disc in the Seal's own colour with a darker edge and a white ring inside it, and on it the Seal's sign, a shape of the battle effects' atlas (`FxTextures`: a heart, a star, a slash, a puff, a spark, a bubble, a flame, a streamer, a petal, a note, a drop, a burst, a twinkle) or, for a letter Seal, its letter in the interface's type. The sign is white on a deep colour and a deep tone of the Seal's own on a pale one (`StickerInk`), so a white smoke and a yellow star keep their colours. A family's A to G differ in colour.
  - **A capsule** (`CapsuleBall`): a ball in the Disc colour with a rule across its middle and a white button on it, a soft edge under it, and its stickers where they were stuck, the first in front. The ball's radius is the capsule's sixty dots.
  - **The twelve**: titled BALL CAPSULES. A panel 1000 wide with four cards to a row, three rows, each a capsule with its number at the top left and, when it is on a ball, its Pokémon's menu icon on a white disc at the lower right; the cursor is the Red frame. Under it the prompt's panel (96 tall), one line. On the right the capsule under the cursor: BALL CAPSULE and No. N, a tag of how many Seals of eight, the capsule large, ON THE BALL OF and the Pokémon's name (or a line in Muted when it is on none), and at the foot the stage. A capsule's menu is the choice box beside its card.
  - **The stage** (`CapsuleStage`): a panel in the night's dark (40 46 78 down to 26 30 54) with a mound of ground, the Pokémon's menu sprite at 2× (the first of the team's for a capsule on none), and the capsule's burst drawn flat round it, clipped to the panel, played over and over with a rest of 1.3 s between. It is the battle's burst piece for piece (`SealFx.Motes`), a dot of the capsule 1.6 layout units.
  - **Changing one**: on the left the case, SEAL CASE with the page of pages on a tag, eight kinds to a page as cards two to a row (the sticker, the name, how many are left, faded at none), and under it the prompt (the Seal's name, what to do). On the right the capsule's panel: the ball at radius 264 inside a pale ring that marks how far a Seal can be carried, the Seal in hand a little larger with a Red ring, faded where it would fall off the ball; a tall stage at the right edge, captioned COMES OUT LIKE THIS. The hints say what the buttons do in each step (Pick up, Stick, Case or Ball, Keep).
  - **The merchant's stall**: titled SEAL MERCHANT, laid out as a shop: TODAY'S SEALS as rows (the sticker, the name, the price), the money's panel at the top right, and under it the Seal under the cursor with a line of what it gives and the stage showing it. "Buy how many?" is the shop's own card (the sticker, the price each, the stepper, the total).
  - **In battle** (`SealFx.Play`): the burst starts as the ball opens and lasts under two seconds; each Seal goes off after the rules' delay, at the rules' place (`CapsuleRules.BurstDelay`, `BurstOffset`), a dot of the capsule the Pokémon's size (held between 0.7 and 1.6) over 80 in the battle's units, round a point a third of the way up its body. Hearts float up and fade; stars are thrown out and fall; lines are rays; smoke is puffs that swell; sparks jump; bubbles rise and wobble; flames lick upward; a party Seal throws streamers; petals drift down turning; notes bob away; drops arc and fall; a burst is a bang with a ring; a twinkle is four-pointed glints. A letter is its 5×7 sign in dots of one colour, four across, which hang for a moment and scatter; letters read from the left for whoever looks at the screen, on either side. Additive pieces (sparks, flames, twinkles) are kept small: they bloom.
- **Ribbons** (plan 06 · R17b; `ModernUi.Ribbons.cs`). A ribbon's sign (`RibbonIcon`) is one of four shapes by what it is given for, in a colour of its own, never the games' picture: a **rosette** (a ring of petals round a gold button with a white star, two tails) for the Super Contests' and the Syndicate's, in the contest's colour, with one to four gold pips under it for the rank; a **bow** (two loops, a knot, two tails) for Hoenn's contests and Julia's seven; a **medal** (a disc on a V of band) for a Champion's, the Battle Tower's and the Footprint Ribbon, whose disc carries a paw; a **pennant** (a swallow-tailed strip with a star) for the Effort Ribbon and those only an event gave.
  - **The summary's page**: the right-hand side as one panel, RIBBONS with the count on a tag, then the Pokémon's ribbons in the original's order as cards six to a row, three rows in view. The button puts a cursor on them (the Red frame); the panel's foot is then a strip in the Disc colour with the ribbon's sign, its name at 40 and a line of what it is for in Muted at 28. A Pokémon with none shows one line in Muted in the panel's middle.
- **The Fashion Case** (plan 06 · R17b; `FashionCaseScreen`, `ModernUi.Ribbons.cs`): titled FASHION CASE, two tabs, ACCESSORIES (Pink) and BACKDROPS (Blue), the item button or left and right changing between them. The accessories are a grid eight wide of their signs (R17a's `AccessoryIcon`) with how many of each; the backdrops a list of eight rows, each a swatch of the backdrop and its name. On the right the thing under the cursor, large (a backdrop as a picture across the panel, 0.72 as tall as wide), its name, a tag of which of how many, and under a rule the contests' records: ENTERED, WON, RIBBONS, captions over numbers.
  - **A backdrop** (`BackdropArt`): a scene of our own for each of the eighteen, by its name: a sky or a wall in two tones and a few plain shapes (a stage's curtains and boards, a town's roofs under snow, a ringed planet among stars, clouds, dunes and a sun, a meadow's flowers, a grid of light, a sea with the sun on it, a dark with one faint rise). Small motifs scale with the picture, so the swatch reads as the large one does.
  - **A photograph** (`DrawContestPhoto`): titled CONTEST PHOTO. A cream print 1040 wide over the screen's backdrop, its picture (640 tall) inside a dark line: the Theater backdrop (red curtains at both sides, a cone of light, boards) under a wash of the contest's colour, the winner's menu sprite at 4×, the accessories it wore round it, and its ribbon's rosette in the top right corner. Under the picture the Pokémon's name at 52, the contest and rank in Muted at 32, and at the right the trainer's name and the day.
- **Pokédex** (`PokedexScreen`, plan 03 · D10): the list on the left, the entry on the right.
  - **HERE** (plan 10 · F11; `PokedexFocus.Here`, `ModernUi.DexHere`): with the DexNav in the bag, the Item button (C; the pad's item button) opens a panel over the dimmed Pokédex, from the list or an entry: 1240 wide and centred, as tall as its rows (six at most, scrolling). At its top the tag LIVES HERE in Green and the shown name of the place the player stands in beside it (Black 44 Ink), then a rule, then the species met at the player's tile at this hour (the grass of the hour, the water and the rods), each a list row 80 high on a 92 pitch: its menu icon (a Rule-coloured dot while unseen), its name in 34 (dashes in Muted while unseen), how it is met in 26 ExtraBold from 520 in (`WaysOf`, the area page's words), its search level in 24 Black ("Search level 3") before the right edge, and a red ball in a disc at the right edge once caught. Up and Down move the cursor (wrapping); A opens the entry of a species the Pokédex has seen (in the Pokédex that has it, its cry played) and buzzes on one it hasn't; B shuts the panel to where it was opened; Start does nothing in it. Where nothing is known to live, the panel's middle says so (the kit's empty note). The list's hint line gains "C Here" while the DexNav is in the bag; without it the Pokédex is as it was.
  - **The list**: the open Pokédex's name on a tag (SINNOH in Blue, KANTO in Green, NATIONAL in Red; a Pokédex a later region brings takes Gold until its plan gives it a colour of its own, plan 18 · W3; **the Pokédex of our own** (plan 40 · GE4) is OF OUR OWN in the FORMS teal, 32 160 168) beside its SEEN and CAUGHT counts, which count that Pokédex's species only. A row has the number in that Pokédex, the name (dashes until it has been seen), the menu icon, and a small ball for a species that has been caught. **A species of our own in the National list** (plan 40 · GE4, D14's heading of ours) wears a pill OURS: teal, 24 tall, the word in Black capitals at 0.58 of that, its right end 196 from the row's right edge (before the research level's disc and the footprint), on every row of ours; a list ordered by size, whose value stands there, shows none. The Pokédex of ours numbers them from 1 in National order. Left and right jump ten. Any of the four keys held down runs on through the list, a species or ten at a time, and up and down held in an entry run through the species seen.
  - **The entry**: four pages chosen with tabs across the panel's top, INFO (Blue), AREA (Green), CRY (violet, 142 96 200, with the radio icon) and SIZE (Gold), and a fifth, FORMS (teal, 32 160 168, with the letter icon), once Professor Rowan's aide has upgraded the Pokédex (plan 08 · P19), and last TASKS (plum, plan 15 · E2), always. The A button moves into the entry: left and right then change page, up and down go to the previous or next species seen, and B goes back to the list, which shows INFO again.
  - **INFO**: a species that has been caught shows everything: its sprite at 3× on a disc, number, name, category, types, height, weight, habitat and its entry. One only seen shows its sprite, name and types, with question marks for the rest. One not yet seen is a question mark on the disc.
  - **Footprints** (plan 09 · L9, `Footprints`): a caught species that walks (a biped, a quadruped or a bird) has a print of its own, made from its model and never the games': one foot's sole seen from below, 16 by 16 texels with its longer side 12, the toes at the top, at least 8 texels inked; a fish, a floater and a serpent leave none, and nothing stands in its place. On INFO it is a stamp at the disc's lower right, its middle 160 right of and 150 below the disc's: a white square 76 across, corners 14, with a Rule rim of 3, and the print at 4× in Ink. In the list it stands 96 from the row's right edge, before the caught ball, at 2× in Muted (White on the chosen row). The opened entry asks for its print (its model is built in the background the first time, and the print is kept beside the menu sprites); the list and the INFO page beside it show only a print already made, so scrolling builds no model, and nothing is drawn until it is.
    - **Height, weight and habitat** (plan 08 · P13) are three captions over values in Black 34, 44 apart, under the types: HEIGHT, WEIGHT and HABITAT, the last one of Platinum's nine kinds of place in words (Grassland, Forest, Water's edge, Sea, Cave, Mountain, Rough terrain, Urban, Rare). Where the habitat's word wouldn't fit before the panel's inner edge it is set smaller (never under 22). A species the entries file lacks has no HABITAT, and HEIGHT and WEIGHT stand as they did, 64 apart.
    - **The written entry** (`Data/dex-entries.json`, plan 08 · P13) is Bold 30 on 42 leading under the rule, in Ink. A species has two, shown in turn: the first until its entry has been opened, then the next at each opening (A from the list, or a step up or down to it within an entry), round its two, for as long as the game runs. The rule ends early on the right to make room for a dot for each entry (8 in radius, 26 apart), the one shown in Blue and the other in the Rule colour. A species the file lacks shows the sentence generated from the data and no dots. The starters' briefcase shows the first entry of each.
  - **AREA**: the map of the Pokédex's region (the listing's own, and for the National listing the region the player stands in, as Platinum shows Sinnoh for everything; a region with no habitats file says "Area unknown." for every species) as the Town Map paints it (below: the half-size picture, smoothed, 20 units to a chunk, in its dark frame), with the cells the species lives in lit in the Selection colour under a white edge, breathing gently. Beside the map, its places in the order of the region, each with how it is met under its name: grass at which times of day, surfing, and with which rods. A species found nowhere says "Area unknown." Swarms, the Poké Radar and the species a second game in the console calls up are left out, as Platinum leaves them out.
  - **CRY** (plan 08 · P18): a species seen. A scope across the top of the panel (a card 220 tall) draws the cry as it is shaped now, as columns 8 wide and 2 apart, each from the wave's lowest to its highest in the column, centred on a rule; the columns the playhead has passed are violet and the rest the Rule's grey, so the scope fills as the cry sounds and stands grey when it does not. Under it five value rows (the Settings' rows: label, a pill, arrows): PITCH (from As it is down to 24 semitones lower and up to 12 higher), STEREO (a CHORUS from Off to 8, or, once A has switched it, a PAN from Left 8 to Right 8), ROOM (a REVERB from Off to 11, or, switched, a FILTER from Off to 11), LOOP and PLAY. A on the cry tab moves into the rows (the hints say Choose, Change, Back), up and down choose a row, left and right change its value in steps and stop at its ends, A on STEREO or ROOM switches what it bends (the amount goes back to nothing for STEREO), A on LOOP turns it, A on PITCH or PLAY plays the cry as shaped, and B goes back to the tabs and stops a loop. A changed value does not play by itself. A loop plays the cry again as it ends, until B. Nothing in the rows is saved: they are as the page was left while the Pokédex is open, and back to nothing when the entry is left. The scope's playhead is the screen's own clock, never the audio thread's, so a shot of it is the same every run.
  - **FORMS** (plan 08 · P19): every look of the species seen, as cards. Across the top the look under the cursor on two discs, from the front (left) and from behind (right), each at 2× on a ball disc 130 in radius, FRONT and BACK under them in Muted 22; beside them the number, the look's name (the species' own or its form's, Black 44, smaller where it would run to three lines), its gender (the sign drawn with `UiShapes`, and the word) and "n seen". A rule and the look's written entry under them (the form's own once it has one, the species' otherwise), with a dot for each of its entries as INFO has, the one shown in teal. Under that the cards, eight to a row and two rows showing (104 square, 114 apart across and 112 down, the menu icon at 2×, the gender sign in the corner), in the order each look was first seen, so Unown's marks stand in the order met; the grid scrolls by rows past two, with a thin bar at its right. A moves into the cards (the hints say Entry, Look, Choose, Back), left and right choose one, wrapping, up and down a row, stopping at the first and the last, A turns to the look's next written entry and B goes back to the tabs. A female seen of a species with a female look is drawn in it, on the discs and on her card (plan 09 · L8, `SeenLook.Look`), which is where a female look is seen in the Pokédex: the INFO page has no toggle. The tab is there for every species seen once the Pokédex is upgraded, so the tabs don't change as up and down go through the species; a species seen in one look shows that one card.
  - **SIZE**: the species and the player as two dark silhouettes (black at 84 %) standing on one line at the heights the Pokédex gives (the player is 1.4 m), the taller of the two 520 tall. They are the one exception to whole-number scales: flat shapes scaled to whatever their heights ask for. Only for species caught, whose height the Pokédex knows.
  - **Search** (the Start button): a panel over the dimmed Pokédex with rows like the options' (a value on a pill between two arrows): which Pokédex (once the player has more than one: the regional Pokédex of each region entered, then the National one once it is open, then the Pokédex of our own, OF OUR OWN, once the National one is open and one species of ours has been seen, plan 40 · GE4), the order (number, A to Z, heaviest, lightest, tallest, smallest), the first letter (A B C, D E F … Y Z; a language with its own groups offers those, plan 17 · N3: the kana rows for Japanese, the initial consonants for Korean, a name filed by the letter it sounds from), two types and a body shape (the Pokédex's fourteen), then SEARCH (Green) and RESET (Blue), and DIPLOMA (Gold) once one has been given. The results replace the list, with how many were found on a tag; B goes back to the whole list. As in Platinum it looks only among the species seen, and the orders by weight and height only among those caught; those orders show the weight or the height at each row's right.
  - **TASKS** (plan 15 · E2): the last page of every species seen, its tab plum (120 72 176) with the star icon. At the top left a disc of radius 64 in plum with the research level in white Black 72 (a Gold disc with Ink figures at level 10), RESEARCH LEVEL as the kit's caption beside it and under that "23 / 35 points" in Black 34 Ink, with a bar 12 tall in plum under the words showing the points over all the rungs. A rule, then a row for each task (as many as the species has, seven or eight, 72 apart from 210 below the panel's top, 62 tall, so the eighth keeps the panel's margin under it, a Card each, none chosen): what it counts in Bold 28 Ink 32 in ("Times met", "Times it used Tackle"), the count over the next target in Black 26 Muted ("3 / 5", or the count alone in Green once the last rung is reached) right of the middle, and at the right edge the rungs as pips (radius 9, 26 apart; one pip for a ladder of one): plum where reached, the Rule's grey where not. A species with nothing counted shows its tasks with every pip grey. The page reads only what the player did and changes nothing in play, so it is there under every rule.
  - **The list's level** (plan 15 · E2): a species with research level 1 or more has its level on a small plum disc (radius 17, white Black 22) 150 from the row's right edge, before the footprint and the ball; on the chosen row the disc is white with plum figures. A list ordered by size, whose value stands there, shows no level.
  - **Diploma**: a cream certificate with a gold double rule over the dimmed screen: DIPLOMA, which Pokédex (SINNOH POKÉDEX, KANTO POKÉDEX, JOHTO POKÉDEX, HOENN POKÉDEX, UNOVA POKÉDEX, the Kalos and Alola ones, NATIONAL POKÉDEX, and for the species of our own POKÉDEX OF OUR OWN: every Pokédex a region has, which plan 40 · GE4 gave their words), the player's name, a line on what was done (in our own words), the day, and a gold seal. It is shown the first time the Pokédex is opened once it is complete, and again from the search panel. **The research certificate** (plan 15 · E1) is the same paper with CERTIFICATE for its heading, SINNOH RESEARCH under it and its own line (every species of the Sinnoh Pokédex researched to level 10); it is shown after any diploma the first time the Pokédex is opened once all 210 reach level 10, once ever, and again from the search panel's DIPLOMA where no diploma of the Pokédex chosen is held.
- **The strip of helpers** (plan 12 · Q18): while the options' FIELD HELPERS row is on and a field move is granted that nobody on the team knows, a line under the six cards, between them and the prompt (y 880, 38 tall): HELPERS in Muted Black 24 at the left, then a pill (`Button`, radius 19, Selection) for each move in 24 ExtraBold capitals, 14 apart. The arrows go down onto it from the last row of cards, left and right along it and up off it; the pill under the cursor is lit as a chosen button is. A pill chosen is the move done by the wild helper, with the same check and the same words as a move of a Pokémon on the team; B comes off the strip before it closes the menu. The cut-in of such a move shows the helper's own front sprite on its band, and the line before it says that a wild Pokémon lent a hand.
- **The party's menus** (plan 02 · S2): choosing a Pokémon on the party's cards opens its menu, a panel 400 wide standing on the prompt over the half of the screen its card isn't on, with a row of 70 for each entry (SUMMARY, its field moves in the order of its moves, SWITCH, CANCEL) like the bag's actions. The prompt then says "Do what with this Pokémon?", and any word the menu has (why a move can't be used here, what Soft-Boiled healed) takes the prompt's place until the next button. While Milk Drink or Soft-Boiled waits for a Pokémon to give HP to, the giver's card keeps the gold of a Pokémon being moved.
- **REMEMBER and RENAME** (plan 12 · Q19): while the options' PARTY TOOLS row is on, a Pokémon's menu has two entries more, REMEMBER and RENAME, between its field moves and SWITCH (none for an Egg; RENAME only for a Pokémon that is the player's own, one that was traded in keeps the name it came with). REMEMBER opens, over the cards' right half, a panel 820 wide in the style of the bag's move pick: MOVES TO REMEMBER in the section-label style and a row of 86 on a pitch of 96 for each level-up move at or below its level that it doesn't know (type pill, name, category and power, its PP), five rows at a time with the cursor's row kept in view; the prompt says "Remember which move?". A on a row teaches it for nothing when a place is free; when the Pokémon knows four, the evolution scene's choice appears in place of the list (the new move on top, the four, a last row that keeps them all) and B there keeps all four and goes back to the list. A Pokémon with nothing to remember says so in the prompt. RENAME opens the introduction's keyboard over the field (the screen's title names the Pokémon, ten letters at most, an empty name giving the species' own back) and closes on the party menu. The options' PARTY TOOLS row (OFF, BY THE RULES, ON; by the rules it is off in a Platinum game and on in a modern one, which is every game played now) goes after FIELD HELPERS.
- **Choosing one of a species** (plan 14 · B14, `choosepokemon "Bidoof"`): the team's own screen in its choosing mode, as entering a team for the Battle Frontier is (`ModernUi.DrawPartyChoice`, the confirm hint CHOOSE). A Pokémon of the species named carries no tag and stays bright; every other card, an Egg among them, carries the grey NOT ABLE where a field move's tag would be and is laid over with a dim film (40 40 60 at 90 of 255, the card's radius), as a fainted card is greyed. A on a dimmed card plays the error sound and puts the refusal in the prompt at the foot ("Only Bidoof can be chosen here.") with nothing chosen. A choice with no species named is the screen as it was.
- **Pokérus** (plan 06 · R10): while a Pokémon carries it, its party card and its summary show a PKRS tag after its status, in a colour of its own that no status or type uses (a deep teal, 16 132 124; a plum read as the poison status beside it). A Pokémon over it shows nothing; the original's small face for a cured one is left out.
- **The Town Map** (`TownMapScreen`, `ModernUi.TownMap.cs`, plan 01 · M12): one screen for the Town Map out of the bag, for Fly's choice (it replaced `FlyScreen`) and for a map on a wall, titled `TOWN MAP`, `FLY` or `MAP`.
  - **The picture** (`RegionMapArt`, painted once and kept by `RegionMapPictures`): the region painted from the world's own tiles, a texel a tile (Sinnoh's is 960 by 768: columns 0 to 29 of the grid, rows 5 to 28), in a palette of its own, with nothing of the original's picture in it. A tile is the colour of what it is: lawn `134,192,106`, tall grass `102,166,88`, a path `228,208,152`, sand `240,226,174`, snow `232,240,248`, paving `200,204,214`, planks `178,134,86`; water in three depths by how far it is from the shore (`94,150,214`, `70,124,198`, `56,104,180`) with a pale line `150,194,234` along the shore itself; forest in pine `50,108,72` or leaf `70,128,62`, a darker and a lighter texel scattered through it, and frosted `104,148,136` in snow country; a mountain in bands of rock from `126,106,90` at its foot to `192,174,152`, capped with snow `230,236,246`, or in bands of ash with a dark crown and embers round a volcano; a building its roof over one row of wall `244,236,214` (a Pokémon Center's red `226,84,78`, a Mart's blue `70,128,220`, a Gym's gold `236,172,60`, a house's by its tiles). Where the world has only the forest, rock and sea that fill the space between its places, and where it has nothing at all, the land's outline is made up: a cell is sea where open water or the map's edge can be come to from it and land otherwise, and every border between two such cells is bent by noise, so no coast follows a chunk's edge. A hidden place is left off (open water, or the land round it) until the story reveals it, and the picture is painted again when it does.
  - **In its frame** (`ModernUi.RegionPicture`): the picture stands in a dark frame `36,44,68`, 8 units wide with round corners. It is drawn point-filtered where a texel is a unit or more, and from its half-size mean, smoothed, where the map is shown smaller. Everything that shows the region draws this: the Town Map, Fly, the Pokédex's area page.
  - **The screen**: the map's panel on the left (1024 by 832, the picture a unit to a tile), the place under the cursor on the right, a strip 56 tall under the map. The cursor is four corners round its cell, white under the Selection colour, which spread as it lands and settle within 0.14 s; it moves a cell at a time within the original's bounds (columns 1 to 28, rows 6 to 28) and a held key repeats. The player is a red pin, a ring widening from its foot once a second. Fly's places are discs of 9 units with a white rim, Gold where the town has been visited and Muted where not. The way come is a white dot with a dark rim on each of the last five cells passed through, one of them in turn larger and with an arrow the way the player faced there (26 thirtieths of a second each, the original's pace); a wall's map has none.
  - **The place**: a tag (ROUTE Green, TOWN or CITY Gold, LANDMARK Blue), beside it VISITED or NOT YET VISITED on one of Fly's places (FLY HERE in the Selection colour when choosing), the name in Black 52 (42 where 52 doesn't fit), a rule, and the place's words in Bold 30 on 42 leading, or "You haven't been here yet." in Muted where the original keeps them back. A landmark in the cell follows once it has been found: a blue disc, its name in Black 36, its words. At the panel's foot a window 256 tall shows the cursor's cell from near, four units to a tile, with white corners round the cell, and under a rule YOU ARE IN with the name of the player's place (indoors, the place the room was come into from).
  - **Zoomed in** (Confirm; the Menu key when choosing where to fly, where Confirm flies): a third of the map at three units to a tile, following the cursor and eased. The window at the foot then shows the whole map small, a white frame round the part on the left, under WHERE THIS IS.
  - **Under the map**: what the marks mean (You, Visited, Not yet visited, The way you came), or "Fly to where?". When choosing, the cells of an open place under the cursor are lit in the Selection colour, breathing; a place that isn't open answers Confirm with the error sound and the map stays.
  - **Fly between regions** (plan 18 · W9): once Fly can reach another region (one the player has been in), the place's panel on the right is topped by a row of `Tabs` 76 tall, one per region in the journey's order (the region's name, a folded-map sign, the region's Pokédex colour: Sinnoh Blue, Kanto Green, Gold for the rest), the panel itself 16 under it and shorter by as much. The open tab is the region whose map is on the left; Q and E (the shoulders) change it, the cursor going to that region's first place and the map whole, with the page sound; a hint "Q / E · Region" joins the others. On another region's tab there is no red pin and no way come, and YOU ARE IN names the player's own place followed by its region ("Jubilife City, Sinnoh"). With one region there is no row and the screen is as above.
  - **The day's report** (plan 38 · EX2, `ModernUi.FieldReport.cs`; the Town Map and the Pokégear's map card, once the region's Hall of Fame is entered): the item button (C) turns the map to its second page and back, and the hint `C Today` stands among the hints only where there is a report, so a map before the Hall of Fame is drawn as ever. On the report's page the map keeps everything of its first page and adds, over every cell of a place with one of the day's outbreaks, a white ring under a Gold one (breathing between 170 and 255 of alpha), and over the visitor's place the same in Blue; over the middle of each such place a disc of the mark's colour on a white rim (at least 22 across, about a cell) holds the species' menu icon at a whole scale. A swarm and a roamer are still not marked. The strip under the map is the legend: a Gold ring "Outbreak", a Blue ring "Visitor". The place's panel becomes the list: a Gold `TODAY` tag, "Today in Sinnoh" in Black 46, a rule, `OUTBREAKS` and a row for each (the icon on a white disc ringed in the mark's colour, 68 across, the species in Black 34 and the place in ExtraBold 26 Muted beside it, and a tag "12 met" in the mark's colour at the right), a rule, `VISITOR` and the visitor's row ("47 min left" in Blue), or the scan's line in ExtraBold 30 (Ink when a scan may be made, Muted when it can't: no National Pokédex, scanned today, nothing calls). A scan is the A button on this page, with the select sound, or the error sound where it can't; a toast says who came and where.
  - **The hour's rift on the report** (plan 38 · EX5): every cell of the rift's place has a white ring under a violet one (`150,96,220`), breathing as the others do; the legend gains a violet ring "Rift"; and the list ends, under a rule, with `RIFT`, a violet disc with a white ring and a violet middle, the place in Black 34 and a violet tag "until 15:00".
- **Trainer Card** (`TrainerCardScreen`, plan 06 · R12): one wide card that rises into place, in the colour of what the player has done (`TrainerCardRules.CardColour`, `ModernUi.CardColor`; our own palette, never the games'): slate without a Pokédex, then teal, cobalt, bronze, silver, gold and black. The colour is the band across the card's top and the card's edge (a frame 6 wide in the colour darkened, with a thin line of it lightened inside); the paper stays the panels' white. The band carries the name, the five stars (each done a gold star with a dark edge, the rest in the band's colour lightened, opaque so their overlapping parts don't show) and the ID number.
  - **The house of titles' title** (plan 37 · FC4): on Kalos's region page, once a title is earned, "House of titles: <title>" in 26 ExtraBold Muted, right-aligned to the card's inner edge, just under the rule beneath the region's name (top + 124), above the badges.
  - **The front**: money, time played, the Pokédex's caught and seen counts and the score on the left (captions over values, two to a row), the player's own field sprite at 6× on a pale plate (touched with the card's colour) on the right, and along the bottom the badges of the region the player is in (plan 18 · W2: its own count, none to eight, from its file) at radius 44 with their names, at the eight places' spacing from the left, so a region of eight fills the row as Sinnoh's always has. The caption says how many of how many (BADGES 3 / 8). Once the player has been in a second region, the region's name in capitals stands in Muted at the caption's right end; with one region, nothing does, and the front is as it was.
  - **The run's page** (plan 13 · V1; `CardPage.Run`, after the back, before the regions' pages): only a game that is not Normal has one. The preset's name in 64 Black, "how this game is played" under it in 28 ExtraBold Muted, a rule, then every choice that differs from Platinum's in two columns 640 apart (the caption over its value, as the front's fields), 120 apart. **Under the Nuzlocke** (plan 13 · V4) the choices go three to a row, a third of the width apart and 96 apart (captions 32, a gap of 48), and the page's foot is the **graveyard**: a rule 196 above the card's bottom, GRAVEYARD and the count in the section-label style, then the last eight fallen four to a row, 68 apart, each its name (the nickname, else the species) in 30 Black Ink over its species, level and place in 20 ExtraBold Muted, cut with an ellipsis to its quarter; with more than eight, "and n more before" at the label's right; with none, "Nobody has fallen yet." in 30 ExtraBold Muted. A run that has ended says RUN OVER in 40 Black Red at the right of the preset's name. **A catch's name**: under the Nuzlocke a Pokémon just caught is named on the same keyboard as RENAME, over the field once it is back, and the keyboard closes on the field. The CONTINUE card's summary and the slot cards carry the same preset as a Selection-coloured tag (`ModernUi.RunMark`): on the summary at the right end of the badges' row, on a short card at the right end of its foot line; a Normal game has no mark and its cards are as they were. **A run that has ended** (plan 13 · V5) wears the same tag in Red with RUN OVER in its place (`ModernUi.SaveMark`). **The options** (plan 13 · V5): while the run keeps the battle style on Set, the BATTLE STYLE row reads Set whatever the file holds, its help line says the run keeps it, and a press on it plays the error sound and changes nothing.
  - **A page per region** (plan 18 · W2, decision 1; `CardPage.Region`): once the player has been in two regions or more (their Pokédex entered, a badge won there, or standing there now), left and right go through the card's pages: the front, the back, then a page for each region in the journey's order. A page turns like the back (0.3 s narrowing to the edge). Its top, under the band, is the region's name in Black 64 with the region's badges counted beside it in Muted (3 / 8) and CHAMPION in a gold pill once its Hall of Fame is entered; then the region's medallions in a row at radius 56, on the front's spacing scaled to fit, with their names in Black 24 under them (Muted until won); a region with no badges says so in a line of Muted. Under a rule, once the player has the region's own Pokédex (plan 18 · W3), how many of its species are seen and caught, captions over values as on the front (SEEN IN ITS POKÉDEX 24 / 151). The hints add "← → PAGE" only while there are such pages.
  - **The statistics page** (plan 13 · V8; `CardPage.Statistics`, after the run's page and before the titles'): only once the game has counted anything. Four `Tabs` under the band (Journey, Battles, Catching, Growth; icons Pin, Boost, Ball, Star in Blue, Red, Green, Gold), the group's counters under them three to a row on a pitch of 140, each a Label caption over its number in Black 48 Ink; up and down change the tab (`TrainerCardScreen.StatTab`), left and right turn pages as ever.
  - **The medals' page** (plan 13 · V9; `CardPage.Medals`, after the statistics page and before the titles'): there once anything is counted or a medal is won. Six `Tabs` under the band (Journey, Battles, Catching, Growth, Pokédex, League; icons Pin, Boost, Ball, Star, Dex, Trainer in Blue, Red, Green, Gold, Red, Blue), the category's medals under them in three columns a third of the width each and rows on a pitch of 64, filled across: each a small medal (a disc of radius 20, Gold with its darker rim and a white star once won, the Rule colour with a Muted rim and no star before), its name in 24 Black (Ink once won, Muted before) and under it a bar 8 high to the cell's right end less 24, filled in Gold as far as its count goes, with the count and the goal in 18 ExtraBold Muted right-aligned over the bar's end (340 / 1,000; WON in Gold once won). Eight rows, so a category holds 24 medals at most (`Medals.MostInACategory`, which a test holds). Along the card's foot, right-aligned 72 in, MEDALS and the count won of all in 24 Black Muted (12 / 120). Up and down change the tab (`TrainerCardScreen.MedalTab`).
  - **The titles' page** (plan 14 · B15; `CardPage.Titles`, after the run's page and before the regions'): only once a title of the Masters' Hall is won. MASTER TITLES in Black 64 with the count beside it in Muted (12 / 1025), "won in the Masters' Hall" under it in 28 ExtraBold Muted, a rule, then the titles by National Pokédex number, three to a row on a pitch of 50 and nine rows at once (`ModernUi.CardTitleRows`, which `TrainerCardScreen.TitleRows` is held to): each the number in muted Black 20 (No. 0399) and the title in Black 28 Ink, cut with an ellipsis to its column. Up and down move the list a row while the page is shown; when the list is longer than the page, where it stands (1–27 of 40) is written right-aligned on the subtitle's line in muted Black 24. The latest title won stands under the player's name in the band on the front only, in capitals in Black 24, the band's colour lightened, the name rising 16 to make room; with no title the band is as it was.
  - **The back** (the A button turns the card over: in 0.3 s it narrows to its edge and opens on the other side): a ledger of five rows 88 apart, each caption on the left and its value right-aligned in Black 40, with a rule between rows: the Hall of Fame debut (the day and the time, or a dash before it), link battles won and lost, link trades, the Super Contests won of those entered (plan 06 · R17b), and the day the adventure began. The stars again at its foot with how many of five. Platinum's signature is left out; a card the player signed (plan 43 · LF1) has the signature in the note's place at the foot's right, ink (30 40 90) on a box 480 by 144 (three units a cell) over a Rule line 10 above its foot.
  - **The designed front** (plan 43 · LF1; `TrainerCardInfo.Design`, `ModernUi.CardFrontDesigned`): a card the player designed keeps its band, its edge in the earned colour and its badges' row; between the band and the badges (150 under the card's top, 1468 by 420) the design's face (`CardFaceArt`): the background's two tones top to bottom with its pattern in its ink (or a Fashion Case backdrop as the Fashion Case paints it), the frame round it, the posed figure on the left (360 wide, on a soft shadow), the greeting in quotes in 44 Black Ink on a white pill (alpha 170) at 410 from its left, and under it the six shown as icons at 2× on white discs of radius 48, 106 apart; the records (money, time, caught, seen, score; captions over values at 34) on a white plate (alpha 214) 400 wide at the face's right. The title chosen stands under the name in the band as the master's title always has. A card never designed is drawn exactly as it was.
  - **The card designer** (plan 43 · LF1; `CardDesignScreen`, `ModernUi.CardDesign.cs`): opened by the item button on the card, titled CARD DESIGNER. On the left a panel 620 wide of nine rows 88 apart (BACKGROUND, FRAME, POSE, FACE, TITLE, SHOWN, GREETING, SIGNATURE, PRINT), each a `ListRow` with its name at 26 and its value right-aligned in 26 Black (smaller to fit 55% of the row); on the right the face as the card will have it, at the panel's remaining width (scale 1140 / 1468), with the title under the greeting. Under it: for SHOWN, THE SIX SHOWN and a list of the team and the boxes, eight rows 66 apart, each the icon, the name, the level and a green dot for one shown (a ring for one not); for SIGNATURE, SIGN YOUR CARD and the pad, 160 by 48 cells at six units a cell on a cream card (252 250 244) with a Rule line near its foot, the pen a ring of 12 (Blue, Red as an eraser), filled while it writes, and PEN or ERASER with WRITING or LIFTED under it; otherwise the signature, if any, on a cream plate at the preview's right. Backgrounds (twenty of ours) and frames (eight) are `Data/card-looks.json`'s, each waiting for what its `unlock` names (the start, a region's Badges or Hall of Fame, a category of medals whole, a diploma, a region's photo spots); the Fashion Case's backdrops owned follow the file's backgrounds. The figure is the player as the field draws them (the grown kit from Kalos on) rendered posed (a gesture held at 45% of its round, or sitting on the ground) and still, with the face chosen.
