- **The photo book** (plan 43 · LF4; `PhotoBookScreen`, `ModernUi.PhotoBook.cs`): titled PHOTO BOOK; `Tabs` of ALL (blue, a star) and each region with a photograph (green, a pin); a page of six photographs three across, each on a cream mat 560 by 392 (rows 20 apart) (252 248 238 to 240 234 220) with its picture 16:9 at its top, its place (a spot's name, else the map's) in 26 Black and its day in 20 Bold Muted under it, PHOTO SPOT in a Gold tag at the right for a spot's; the one under the cursor ringed in Selection 6 wide; PAGE n / m and the count of 100 in 24 Black at the foot's right. A opens one large (1520 by 855) on a cream mat over the page dimmed to 190, its place and day under it; the item button there asks "Throw this photograph away?" with THROW AWAY in Red and NO in Blue, NO first. A photograph whose file is gone is a stand-in card of sky and ground with a camera on it and "The picture's file is gone." Its pictures are loaded for the page in view only.
- **The photo studio** (plan 43 · LF5; `StudioScreen`, `ModernUi.Studio.cs`; the photographer's corner of the Jubilife Boutique): titled PHOTO STUDIO; the stage at the upper left, sixteen to nine (1120 by 630), and at the right a panel of eight rows 68 high (BACKDROP, POSE, FACE, POKéMON, PLACE, STICKERS, FRAME, TAKE), each its name in 26 and its value at the right, the one under the cursor in a Selection pill. **The stage** is the picture that is taken: a backdrop (the card's two tones and pattern, or a Fashion Case backdrop), a soft floor ellipse, the player's figure (the card's posed figure, 82% of the stage's height) on one side and the Pokémon's sprite (46%) on the other by PLACE, the stickers (Seals as the capsules' screen draws them, 34 at the full picture's size) where they were put, and the frame (the card's, one of its eight styles) over all. **Stickers**: A on STICKERS opens the case's Seals in a row under the stage (ten at a time, the one chosen ringed), A carries one over the stage with the arrows (a share of the picture, 2.5% a press), A puts it down (up to twelve; a Seal is never used up) and B steps back; left on STICKERS takes the last one off. **TAKE** draws the stage alone, filling the screen, for one frame, and keeps it in the photo book at 1920 by 1080 with its small copy, as photo mode's pictures are (the print's place reads "Photo studio").
- **PC boxes** (`PCScreen`, plan 06 · R12): three columns. The party as six compact cards on the left; the box in the middle; on the right the Pokémon under the cursor, or the one being carried (sprite at 2× on a disc, its six marks in a row, name, level, types, HP, nature, moves, where and when it was met, what it holds).
  - **The box**: its panel is lined with the box's wallpaper (below), inside a white rim of 10. The box's own name sits on a pale plate between two arrows at its top, under the plate is what says which box this is (for Platinum's eighteen boxes a row of eighteen small dots 20 apart, the current one in the Selection colour; for a PC with more, the hundred that come with the National Pokédex (plan 03 · D13), a pale pill 560 wide holding a thin track 400 long with a small tick at every tenth box, a knob in the Selection colour where this box stands along it, and at its right end the box's number and how many there are, "35 / 100"), and its thirty places are 6 by 5 plates of translucent white (a white edge, the wallpaper showing through) with each Pokémon as its menu icon at 2×. Under the places, how many the box holds and the wallpaper's name, each on a Frame-coloured tag. The cursor moves from the party into the box and back, and up from the box's top row to its name, where left and right change box (a press goes round from the last box to the first; a key kept down runs on through the boxes and stops at either end); the place under the cursor has the Selection edge and glow.
  - **Carrying** (MOVE): the Pokémon picked up goes with the cursor, its icon at 2× lifted 46 above the place under the cursor and bobbing gently, a soft shadow on the place under it.
  - **Menus**: A on a Pokémon opens what can be done with it (MOVE, WITHDRAW or STORE, MARK, RELEASE, CANCEL), a panel 340 wide with a row of 70 to an entry like the party's menu, beside the place or the party card on the side with more room; A on the box's name opens NAME, WALLPAPER and CANCEL under the plate. WALLPAPER puts up the box's next wallpaper at once.
  - **Marks** (MARK): a panel beside the place with the six marks (circle, triangle, square, heart, star, diamond, drawn with `UiShapes`, `ModernUi.MarkShape`) on keys like the keyboard's, then OK. A mark that is set is Ink on a key tinted with the Selection colour, one that isn't is faint; the cursor is a Selection ring. Marks shown elsewhere (the detail panel) are Ink when set and the Rule colour when not.
  - **Release** (RELEASE): asked over the dimmed screen ("Release Starly?"), RELEASE in Red and KEEP IT in Blue, each naming its key, since the question has no cursor.
  - **Naming** (NAME): the introduction's keyboard, with eight slots, and on the left in place of a portrait the box's wallpaper on a plate with its name over it.
  - **The boxes' tools** (plan 12 · Q26, the options' BOX TOOLS row; with it off the PC is exactly as above). The box's menu gains SEARCH and SORT before CANCEL, and a Pokémon's menu in a box gains SELECT before CANCEL.
    - **Search**: a panel over the box and the detail column (from the box's left edge to the right margin, the content's full height), its title on the left of its head and how many the boxes hold on the right. Its rows are cards 60 high, 6 apart: the filter's name on the left (28, ExtraBold) and its value on a pill 360 wide between two arrows on the right (26, Black), Frame-filled on the chosen row and pale on the others, as the options' rows are made smaller. The rows: species, type, move, ability, nature, lowest level, highest level, held item, gender, shiny, mark; each offers ANY first and then only what the boxes hold, so no choice finds nothing by itself. The last row is SEARCH, a `ListRow` centred.
    - **Results**: the same panel, its head saying how many were found. Its first row is PICK UP ALL with the count; under it a `ListRow` of 64 to each Pokémon found, its menu icon at 1× on the left, its name, and on the right the box's name and the place's number in Muted. Eight show at once and the list scrolls with a scroll bar. A on a result goes to its place; A on PICK UP ALL picks every one up together.
    - **Sort**: two small menus under the box's plate as the box's menu is: first THIS BOX or ALL BOXES, then BY NUMBER, BY LEVEL, BY NAME, BY TYPE, BY RECENT. A box sorted packs its Pokémon from its first place; all boxes sorted are laid out from the first box on, Eggs last. The team is never touched.
    - **A rectangle** (SELECT): the place it was begun on is its corner, the cursor the other, and every place inside is tinted with the Selection colour at a quarter and edged in it; the cursor stays inside the box while a rectangle is drawn. A opens MOVE, RELEASE and CANCEL beside it.
    - **Several carried**: the first three icons stacked over the place under the cursor, each 10 to the right of and 8 above the one before, the first on top, with a Selection pill at their right shoulder counting them ("×12"). They go down into the box under the cursor together, into its empty places from the cursor's on and round to its first, in the order they were picked up; a box without room for all of them refuses them all, and nothing goes onto the team. B puts each back where it came from.
    - **Release of several**: the release question as a single release asks it, naming how many ("Release these 3?"), RELEASE in Red and KEEP THEM in Blue. Its yes waits for the same hold as a single release (Q9's `ConfirmHold`, "Holding to confirm") where the options' HOLD TO CONFIRM row is on, its ring filling on RELEASE; with the row off a press lets them go.
    - **Teams** (plan 12 · Q27): the box's menu gains TEAMS after SORT. Its panel is the search's (over the box and the detail column), titled TEAMS with how many are kept on the right ("2 / 8"). Each team kept is a card 84 high, 6 apart: its name on the left (30, Black) and its six places in a row on the right, each a menu icon at 1× on a small disc (Disc colour, 30 across), a place whose Pokémon is no longer stored an empty Rule ring. The last row is SAVE THIS TEAM, a `ListRow` centred, left out once eight are kept. A on a team opens SWAP IN, DELETE and CANCEL beside it as a menu panel; A on SAVE THIS TEAM opens the keyboard with ten slots and the team's six icons on the left plate in place of a wallpaper. A swap is at once: the team's members come onto the team in its order, healed as anything withdrawn is, and the team that was there goes into the places they left, then into the first empty places; what can't be swapped (no member stored, a team member holding Mail or a capsule that would go into a box, too little room) is refused whole with a notice, and a member no longer stored is skipped with one ("Starly isn't in the boxes any more.").
    - **The judge** (plan 12 · Q27): a Pokémon's menu gains JUDGE before CANCEL (after SELECT in a box), which turns the detail column into the judge's page until B: the sprite, marks and name as before, then JUDGE as a label and the six stats as rows 56 apart, each the stat's name on the left (26, ExtraBold) and on the right a pill of the word for its IV in Black 24: NOTHING (Rule, Ink), MEAGRE, FAIR, GOOD (Blue, white), SPLENDID (Green, white), FLAWLESS (Gold, Ink); the numbers themselves are never shown. Under them a line in Muted that names the best stat ("Best: Speed"), or "All alike" where they are one word. An Egg is judged NOTHING TO SEE. The cursor moves through the boxes with the page up, so a box can be judged place by place.
- **Box wallpapers** (`BoxWallpapers`): Platinum's twenty-four in its order (Forest, City, Desert, Savanna, Crag, Volcano, Snow, Cave, Beach, Seafloor, River, Sky, Poké Center, Machine, Checks, Simple; then Distortion, Contest, Nostalgic, Croagunk, Trio, PikaPika, Legend and Team Galactic, which a word game unlocks), each painted in code as a soft gradient with one simple motif of our own: fir trees, a skyline, dunes, a flat-crowned tree against the sun, angular rocks, a cone and its smoke, snowflakes and drifts, stalactites, sea and sand, bubbles and weed, a winding river, clouds, rounded crosses, circuit lines, a checkerboard, a plain inner line; rings that waver, rosettes, blocky four-tone squares, orange discs on blue, three overlapping discs in yellow, pink and blue, zigzags and red dots, a diamond, a pearl and a hexagon, orbits and stars. Never the games' pictures. They are painted at half the layout's size (`BoxWallpapers.Paint`) and drawn smoothed, and they are quiet so that the icons read on all of them: no pixel darker than 0.4 in lightness (its sRGB luma), and the darkest and the lightest of one wallpaper within 0.36 of each other (`MenuScreenTests`).
- **Hall of Fame** (`HallOfFameScreen`, plan 06 · R12): one panel. Its head is the team's number on a Gold tag, the day it entered, and on the right which of the teams kept this is. Under it the team as six cards in a row (sprite at 2× on a disc, nickname, level; an empty place a pale slot), the chosen one with a Gold edge and glow and hopping. Under the cards two tabs, PROFILE and MOVES (the A button turns between them): the nickname large with its gender mark and a gold star for a shiny one, then species, level, original trainer and ID No. as captions over values, and the species' types on pills; or its four moves on pills of their types' colours, two by two. Once two regions have teams in it (plan 41 · EG2) a row of tabs (`Tabs`, 64 tall, at most five in view round the one shown, 208 each, each its region's laurel colour from "The hub's halls" with a star, EARLIER TEAMS bronze) stands at the head's right in place of which team it is, which moves to its left; the shoulders turn them (Q / E, its hint first in the corner), and the number and the count are the tab's.
- **Journal** (`JournalScreen`, plan 06 · R12): a page of a notebook on the backdrop: cream paper (252 248 236 at the top to 244 237 218), ruled in pale blue every 70, a soft red margin line, and the spiral's rings down its left edge. Its day at the top (the weekday in small capitals over the date in Black 48), then the page's lines in Ink, each on its rule, a long one carried on to the next. The day's Pokémon is a photograph pinned at the top right: its sprite at 2× on a white card held by a strip of tape, its name under it (pixel art is never turned). At the foot, the page's number of how many there are between arrows that show only where there is another page (older to the left).
- **Vs. Recorder** (`ReplayListScreen`, `ModernUi.Records.cs`, plan 08 · P11): the recordings kept in `replays` beside the saves, newest first, titled VS. RECORDER. On the left a list panel 1040 wide of nine list rows: each the first foe Pokémon's icon on its disc, the foe (a trainer's class and name, or the wild Pokémon's species) and the day it was fought as the value; a file the game can't play (damaged, of another game's data, of a newer shape) is a Muted row with its file's name and CAN'T PLAY as the value. On the right the detail panel of the row under the cursor: the foe in Black 44, the day under it, a tag of how it ended (WON Green, LOST Red, anything else Frame) beside the format and the turns, a rule, then THEIR TEAM and YOUR TEAM as section labels over the two teams' icons at 2× in a row; for a file that can't be played its name and the reason (`BattleRecordFile.Notice`) in Bold 30 on 42 leading. With no recordings the list panel says so (`EmptyNote`) and the detail panel is left out. A watches, B goes back to where the Vs. Recorder was used (the bag, or the field from the item button).
- **Watching** (plan 08 · P11): a recording plays as the battle it was, with nobody choosing: no menu opens, and each line stands 1.4 s and goes on by itself. A tag at the top right (the foe's box is at the top left) says so: a Blue pill REPLAY with the foe beside it in Bold 28, a Gold pill ×3 after it while Run is held, and under it the two hints (Menu: LEAVE, Run held: ×3). Run held plays everything at three times the pace and never leaves; Menu leaves at any line. Into it and out of it the screen fades as a door does (no way into a battle), the music is the battle's own, and the stage is the arena of the ground it was fought on. Nothing of the game changes: the Pokémon, bag and Pokédex on screen are the recording's own.
- **The photo album** (`PhotoAlbumScreen`, `ModernUi.Photos.cs`, plan 20, part q): titled PHOTO ALBUM, opened at Cianwood City's studio; one panel the content's width with "n / 36" as a Frame tag in its top right and PAGE n OF m as its label. A page is one photograph: a print 760 by 520 on a cream border (246, 244, 236) 24 thick with 120 more under it for the caption; the print is the scene of the kind of place it was taken in (plan 20, part t; `Models/PhotoScenes.cs`: a town, a route, a forest, the shore, a mountain, a cave or a room, each a sky over a ground from its horizon, about 62% down, and a few flat kit shapes, rectangles, circles and triangles standing on their bases, given in shares of the print; a route is the old plain sky (150, 196, 232) over grass (126, 170, 102)), the lead's menu sprite at three times its size standing on the horizon at 58% across, and the player's figure of kit shapes at 34% (a cap, a face, a coat and two legs, about 170 tall; a boy's cap blue (56, 88, 168), a girl's red (206, 66, 76)); where nobody led the figure stands in the middle and "Just you, smiling" is written 60 above the print's foot. A place card in the print's top left, cream (246, 244, 236, 230) and 40 tall, with a red pin, names the region in Black 22: JOHTO, or KANTO for HeartGold's Kanto. Under the print the place at 34 Black in Ink and the day at 24 ExtraBold in Muted. Left and Right turn the pages, wrapping; A asks "Throw this photograph away?" on a Frame bar 64 tall at the panel's foot, a second A throws it away and B keeps it. An empty album says so in Muted 30 in the panel's middle. Our own look: HeartGold's frames and poses are not copied.
- **Jukebox** (`JukeboxScreen`, `ModernUi.Jukebox.cs`, plan 12 · Q15): titled JUKEBOX, opened from the title's JUKEBOX row and the options' JUKEBOX row; one list and one detail panel under a strip of four tabs (SONGS Blue, SOUNDS Red, CRIES Green, AMBIENCE Gold; Left and Right change the tab, the cursor of each tab is kept). The list is 1000 wide, rows as the other lists (32 Black, a name from 52); a song shows its region (sinnoh, common…) as the value in Muted 24, and the one playing a small disc with the radio sign in its tab's colour at the row's right. **A song not heard yet reads ???** in Muted and cannot be played (the error sound); once the game has played it (`AudioManager.Heard`, kept in the save; on the title the union of the saves' songs) it is its title. The detail panel (the rest of the width): the name at 46 Black, "n of total" under it, a rule, then, for a song, the region as a Frame tag, its length ("Loops, 1:12 a round" or "Plays once, 0:04") and its ARRANGEMENTS as tags 44 tall, the one the shoulder keys (Q and E) chose in the tab's colour and the others Muted: DAY always, NIGHT only where the song has a night arrangement, LOW HP only where it has the battle's low-HP one; a song not heard offers the day alone, so it tells nothing of itself. For a sound the group tag (MENU, FIELD, BATTLE, MOVE) and where the game plays it; for a cry the species chosen (Q and E, in National order) with its number and a line on the mode; for a bed where the game plays it. The A button plays what is under the cursor; a song or a bed played again stops; B goes back and puts the music of the place back.
- **Gallery** (`GalleryScreen`, `ModernUi.Gallery.cs`, plan 12 · Q30): titled GALLERY, opened from the title's GALLERY row and from a Pokédex entry (A on its INFO, AREA or SIZE page, whose hints gain "A Gallery"); two tabs (POKéMON Green, PEOPLE Blue; Enter changes the tab). On the left a list 620 wide of what may be shown, rows as the other lists: on POKéMON every species seen in National order, each followed by every form it has (indented 32, in Muted until chosen), on PEOPLE the player's two looks and the professor. The rest of the width is the stage (the model, see "Pokémon in menus"), with along its foot a strip of four tags 44 tall: the clip (or the emote) in the tab's colour, FRONT or BACK, the zoom (1×, 1.5×, 2×) and, while the model is still being built, BUILDING in Muted. The name stands over the stage's top left in white at 46 Black, with its number under it in pale blue (196, 206, 236) at 26 ExtraBold. Keys: Up and Down the row (a key held runs on), Left and Right turn the model (held, a half turn a second; the turning on its own stops), C the next clip or emote, P front or back, the shoulders (Q and E) the zoom, A the cry (and the clip again from its start; on PEOPLE the emote again), B back. Opened from a Pokédex entry it opens on that species and B goes back to the entry.
- **The battle room** (`BattleRoomScreen`, `TeamBuilderScreen`, `ModernUi.BattleRoom.cs`, plan 13 · V13, V26): the title's BATTLE ROOM, titled BATTLE ROOM, the Vs. Recorder's two panels (a list 1040 wide on the left, the detail panel taking the rest). Its menu is four rows (TEAMS, GAUNTLET, STREAK, PUZZLES; `ListRow` with the name from 44 and no icon), each with its record as the value in Black 28 (the teams kept, "Best n/13", "Best n", "n/10 solved"); the detail panel names the row in Black 44 over a rule and a paragraph of what it is in Bold 30 on 42, with "Cleared n times" in a Gold tag at its foot once the gauntlet has been won. GAUNTLET and STREAK open the list of teams ("Which team…?"): each team's first member's icon on its disc, its name, "n Pokémon" as the value, a team that can't battle in Muted with its value in Red and refused with the error sound; the detail shows the team's six as icons at 2× on discs 108 across with "Lv. n" under each in Black 24, then READY in a Green tag or CAN'T BATTLE in Red with its reason in Bold 30. PUZZLES lists the puzzles numbered 01 to 10 in Black 28 at the row's left, the title as the name, and a Gold star (`Star`, radius 22) at the right of a solved one; the detail has the title, the goal in a Blue tag and the rules in a Frame tag, a rule, the hint in Bold 30, and THE FOE and YOUR SIDE as section labels over each side's icons at the panel's foot. Between two battles of a run, at its end and after a puzzle, one panel 1120 by 640 in the middle (200 from the top) holds a heading in Black 52 to 56 (Ink between battles, Gold for a gauntlet cleared, Red for a run over, Green "Solved!" and Red "Not this time" for a puzzle), the progress in a Blue tag (NEW BEST beside it in Gold), a rule, YOUR TEAM over the team's icons, the next foe in ExtraBold 32 and its rows (NEXT BATTLE and GIVE UP; OK; TRY AGAIN and BACK TO THE PUZZLES) as list rows along its foot. **The team builder** is the same two panels: TEAM BUILDER's list of teams with NEW TEAM last; a team's page (its name the title) with its members (icon, name, "Lv. n" as the value, a member that can't battle faded with its value in Red), ADD A POKéMON, RENAME (the introduction's keyboard, ten letters) and DELETE TEAM in Red, which asks for a second press as PRESS AGAIN TO DELETE; a member's page of eleven rows in Black 30 (SPECIES, FORM, LEVEL, NATURE, ABILITY, ITEM, four MOVE rows, REMOVE in Red) with the value of each at the right in Black 28, Left and Right changing the level, nature, ability and form and the shoulders the level by ten, A on SPECIES, ITEM or a MOVE opening a list to pick from (species numbered "0445  Garchomp" with their icons, items and moves by name, the first row "None" or "—"; the shoulders jump ten rows); its detail panel is the member's icon at 3× on a disc 176 across, its name in Black 40, its type pills, a rule, its moves as type pills 150 wide with the move's name, its stats in one line of Muted ExtraBold 26, and READY or CAN'T BATTLE with the reason at the panel's foot.
- **The daily run** (`BattleRoomScreen`, `ModernUi.BattleRoom.cs`, plan 13 · V14): the battle room's sixth row, DAILY RUN, its value "Today n/7" once the day has been played and "New today" before. Its detail panel is the room's (name, rule, paragraph) with, at its foot over a rule, the day's date in a Blue tag and "Round n" (the Factory's round of that day of the week) in a Frame tag beside it, then "Today's run is still to play." in Green ExtraBold 32, or "Today's run: n of 7 won" in Ink, and under it the best, the days with all seven and the days played in one line of Muted Bold 28. A pick opens the Battle Factory's own rental screen (its six at level 50, three to choose, "Battle Factory" its summary's place), unchanged; a trade after a win opens its trade screen. Between two battles and at the end it is the room's middle panel, titled DAILY RUN: the heading as a run's ("All seven beaten today!" in Gold for a clear), "n of 7 won" in a Blue tag with PRACTICE in a Frame tag beside it for a second run of the day and NEW BEST in Gold at a counted run's end, YOUR TEAM over the three rented at Lv. 50, the next trainer by class and name, and three rows (NEXT BATTLE, TRADE, GIVE UP), TRADE faded and refused with the error sound once the trade after that win has been made.
- **VERSUS** (`BattleRoomScreen`, `ModernUi.BattleRoom.cs`, plan 13 · V12): the battle room's seventh row, VERSUS, with no value at its right; its detail panel is the room's (name, rule, paragraph). A opens the room's team list twice, titled "Player 1: which team?" and then "Player 2: which team?" (B from the second goes back to the first, from the first to the menu), the detail panel the team's as for a run. The battle is a link battle's on the room's indoor stage, each player's screen from their own side as "Battle panels" has it, with **the curtain** between them.
- **The friends' page** (`FriendsScreen`, `ModernUi.Friends.cs`, plan 13 · V22): the battle room's fifth row, FRIENDS' TRAINERS, opens the same two panels. The list holds, in this order, a row for each trainer file of `trainers/` (its name, READY in the row's Black 26 or REFUSED in Red, a refused one faded and refused with the error sound), READ A CODE, PASTE A TEAM and a row SEND <team> for each built team (its first member's icon). The detail panel of a file is its name in Black 44, its class under it in Muted Bold 28, a rule, its team as the discs of the team pages and a Green READY tag with the one-paragraph blurb of what the fight is (both sides new at level 50, nothing gained or kept); a refused file's is the name, a Red REFUSED tag and its reason in Bold 30, never a word of the file's own. The two readers and SEND show a paragraph of what they do; after SEND the panel shows CODE as a Muted label over the code in ExtraBold 24 on 32, wrapped, which is also on the clipboard. What the last row did is a line in Black 28 at the panel's foot, Green when it went well and Red when it did not.
- **Requests** (`RequestsScreen`, `ModernUi.Requests.cs`, plan 12 · Q21, Q22): titled REQUESTS, opened from the start menu's REQUESTS over the field; the frame of the jukebox: a strip of tabs, OPEN (magenta, the letter), DONE (Green, the star) and, only while the options' WHAT IS LEFT HERE row is on, THIS AREA (Blue, the map card); Left and Right change the tab, the cursor of each kept. OPEN and DONE are one list panel 1000 wide (list rows, 32 Black, a name from 52: the request's title) and the detail panel of the request under the cursor beside it. The pinned request has a small disc with the pin, Gold, at its row's right (white with a Gold pin on the chosen row); a row of DONE says DONE in Green 24 as its value. The detail panel: the title in 44 Black (set smaller to fit), "From the Pokétch Company's president, Jubilife City" under it in 26 ExtraBold Muted (wrapped, 34 leading), a tag PINNED (Gold) or DONE (Green) 38 tall, a rule, STEPS as the kit's caption, then each step on a row of its own: a mark at the left (a Green disc of radius 14 with a white tick for a step done; a 4-wide Selection ring with a Selection dot of 6 in it for the next step; a 3-wide Rule ring for one still to come) and the step's line 48 in, 28 on 36 leading, ExtraBold Ink for the next step and Bold Muted for the others; then REWARD as a caption and what it brings in 28 ExtraBold Ink. An empty list says so in the list panel (`EmptyNote`: nothing asked of you just now; no request done yet) and leaves the detail panel out. **A pins** the request under the cursor on OPEN, or takes the pin off the one pinned (one at a time; the error sound on DONE and THIS AREA); B goes back to the field, keeping what was pinned. **THIS AREA** is one panel the whole content width: the area's name in 48 Black, a rule, and three cards side by side 300 tall (ITEMS Gold, TRAINERS Red, POKéMON Green), each a pale wash of its colour with a 14-high band of it along the top, its caption in 28 Black of its colour darkened three tenths and the count in 120 Black Ink (Muted at nought) in the middle; under them a line in 30 Bold Muted that says nothing is named, or "Nothing left here". Hints: Left / Right Tab, Z Pin (Unpin on the pinned one; on OPEN only), Esc Back.
- **The camp** (`CampScreen`, `ModernUi.Camp.cs`, plan 10 · F6): titled CAMP, opened from the start menu's CAMP. A clearing of our own drawn with the kit's shapes, 1792 by 716 from row 132 (`CampClearing`): a sky fading to a paler blue at its horizon (42 % down), a row of round crowns of trees along it (60 128 92), the lawn in two greens (118 186 96 over 98 168 82), an orange tent with its door open at the back on the left and a fire of three flames in a ring of grey stones at the back on the right. The team stands on it as their menu sprites at 2×, each over a soft shadow: three or fewer in one row across the middle, more in two rows of three, the far row narrower and higher (and drawn first), the near row lower and wider (`CampPlace`). The one under the cursor stands in a Selection ring and breathes (a rise of up to 6 units, about once a second and a half); Confirm opens its close-up (plan 35 · BN2, below; an Egg says it sits warm by the fire); a Pokémon petted hops and three hearts (240 92 128) rise from over its head and fade over 1.4 s, one fed hops under a rising berry the same; an Egg sits still. At the foot a panel 160 tall: the one under the cursor (its name with its gender), FRIENDSHIP as five hearts (one under 70, two to 150, three to 220, four from 220, five at 255; the rest in Rule), PETTED and FED as tags, Green once done this visit and Rule before; and at its right, in ExtraBold 30, the line of what the last thing done came to (the welcome, the cure of a visit after a battle, a pat, a meal, an Egg, a share already had, no berries). Its hints: move, Z to pet, C to feed (the bag opens on its berries and the camp comes back either way), Esc to leave.
- **The care close-up** (plan 35 · BN2; `CareScreen`, `ModernUi.DrawCare`): Confirm on a Pokémon at the camp opens it (an Egg stays warm by the fire). Titled CARE. The clearing behind as the camp draws it, with a veil of Ink at 40 %; the Pokémon front on in a picture 900 units square ("Do and don't"). **The hand** is a white glove of the kit's shapes (a disc 56 across with three finger discs, an Ink rim 3 thick), its place snapped to whole units, moved by the arrows at 900 units a second; held (Confirm) it is drawn pressed (a tenth smaller). **The tray** is a strip of five chips along the foot (HAND, TREAT, BRUSH, CLOTH, MEDICINE, each 200 by 64, 16 apart, the chosen one Selection-filled, the rest Panel), changed with the shoulder buttons (Page left and right). **Marks**: each mark of soil stands on the body's middle (the first a little left, the second a little right) as a cluster of five dots of its colour (dust 196 170 120, mud 112 82 56, leaves 98 168 82, wet 120 176 230, snow 236 240 248), worn away dot by dot as the right tool's strokes wear it; a status shows its pill beside the head. **The liked spot** glows faintly (a ring of Gold at 30 %) while the hand is near it. **The panel** at the top left: the name with its gender, five pips of fullness (Gold eaten, Rule not), and the mood's word (GLUM, LOW, CALM, CHEERFUL, ELATED) in its colour (Red, Gold dimmed, Muted, Green, Gold). The line of what the last thing done came to stands at the top right (from x 1440, 420 wide), in white ExtraBold 30. The hand strokes the body only within the box of its silhouette on the picture, a tenth in from each side (`CareStage.Layout.OnBody`). Hints: move, Z to hold, the shoulders to change the tool, Esc to go back to the camp.
- **The camp's pot** (`CampScreen.Phase`, `ModernUi.DrawCampPot`, plan 39 · CR6, which took plan 10 · F8 over): with the crafting kit in the bag the camp's hints gain Enter to cook. **The pantry** (`CampPhase.Pantry`) is two panels over the clearing, each 820 wide and 560 tall from row 172 (the list's Panel, 34 radius), the clearing still showing between and under them: on the left IN REACH in the small Muted label and five rows (`CampScreen.PantryRows`, the rows the panel's list holds: a sixth would stand below it) of what can go in, a good of the pouch's pantry with its sign or a berry, Honey or a mushroom of the bag with a Frame disc, at 30 ExtraBold, ×held at the right, the cursor's row lit as every list's, and a last row COOK in Green Black; on the right IN THE POT and up to four rows of what is in it, then MAKES in the small label and the dish it would cook (its name 40 Black, its description in Bold 26, wrapping). **On the fire** (`CampPhase.Stirring`) the right panel is the gauge: ROUND n OF 4 in the small label; a pot (a disc 150 across in Ink with a Rule rim, a soup of Gold inside, and a spoon, a Frame stick, standing in it at an angle that turns a quarter at each round); under it a bar 600 by 40 (Rule, 20 radius) with the heat's zone in Red (its width the zone's, `Kitchen.HalfWindow` either side of its middle, so twice as wide under a generous assist) and the needle an Ink rod 10 by 72 with a white cap, its place snapped to whole units; under the bar four discs 36 across for the rounds (Rule while unplayed, Red for a miss, Gold for good, Green for perfect), and the last round's word (MISS, GOOD, PERFECT) at 36 Black in the same colour for half a second. **The dish served** (`CampPhase.Served`) is a card 900 by 400 in the clearing's middle (Panel, 34 radius): the dish's name with its grade before it ("Superb Bean Hotpot") at 48 Black, the grade as four stars in a row (Gold up to it, the rest Rule), "HP restored by N%" in ExtraBold 30, and the power's line ("Encounter Power Lv. 3 for 15 min" in Green ExtraBold 30, or "No power, just a good meal." in Muted); every Pokémon that ate hops under a rising berry as one fed does. Its hints by phase: up and down, A to add (or cook on COOK), Esc to take the last thing out; A to stir, Esc to take the pot off; A to go on.
- **The Week's Work** (`MissionBoardScreen`, `ModernUi.Missions.cs`, plan 41 · EG6): titled THE WEEK'S WORK, opened by reading the board by the hub's avenue or on Pennant Hall's back wall. A list panel 1080 wide holding five rows under a small label of the week ("THE WEEK OF 9 NOV TO 15 NOV"), and the detail panel the rest of the width. **A row** is a list row whose words are what the mission asks ("Catch 3 Water-type Pokémon in Sinnoh.") at 30 Black from 40, cut with an ellipsis to fit 640; a meter (`ModernUi.Bar`, 150 by 20) after it, Blue for a mission of the week, Gold for a timed one and Green once done; and at the right its reward as pennants, or its state in place of the reward: CLAIM! in Green for one done, BEGIN for a timed one not under way, UNDER WAY for the one that is, CLAIMED for one claimed (whose row is dimmed). **The detail panel**: A MISSION OF THE WEEK or A TIMED MISSION in the small label and the region on a Frame tag at the upper right; what it asks in Black 34, wrapped; DONE SO FAR and done/asked at 40, REWARD and the pennants at 40 with "and a Heart Scale" in ExtraBold Muted 26 after them where it pays an item; for a timed one TIME and its minutes (the seconds left, in Gold, while it runs) with a line in ExtraBold Muted 24 saying the clock runs only in the field and battles of its region; at the panel's foot the Battle Hall's foot band with PENNANTS and the pennants held, and HALL RANK and the rank. **The last press's words** ("Claimed! 30 pennants went to Pennant Hall.") wrap in white Black 30 on a band of cork (`196,150,98`) 96 tall under the list. Its hints: up and down to move, A to claim or begin, Esc to go back. A Pennant Hall stall's goods are the Athlete Shop's counter in pennants (the purse PENNANTS, each price the pennant and its number).
- **The kit** (`CraftingScreen`, `ModernUi.Crafting.cs`, plan 39 · CR1): titled CRAFTING KIT, opened from the bag's USE on the Crafting Kit, the item button or a script's `craft open`. A strip of tabs 76 tall under the title (the Jukebox's, so a tab is a pill with its sign on a chip in its colour; MATERIALS in Gold with the pouch's sign, and later MAKE, TMS and CROSSES as their sessions land; Left and Right change the tab, which with one does nothing), then a list 860 wide and a detail panel the rest of the width, as the Apricorn Box has them. **The list** has a row for every material the game has, in the table's order (a Pokémon's by type, the field's, the pantry's), rows as the other lists (32 Black, the name from 112): a sign on its left, the name and ×count at the right; a material not held is dimmed (Muted) but listed, so the player can see what there is to find, and the cursor starts on the first one held. **A material's sign** is a lump drawn with shapes (`MaterialSign`): a disc in its colour (its type's colour for a Pokémon's, a wood brown `176,132,84` for the field's, a green `110,168,88` for the pantry's) with a rim darker by a half, a pale highlight on its upper left, and for a rare one a small cross-shaped glint at the upper right; unheld it takes the same dimming as the Apricorn Box's fruit. **The detail panel**: the group (FROM POKéMON, FROM THE FIELD, FROM THE PANTRY) in the small Muted label at the upper left and, at the upper right, the number of kinds held as a Frame tag ("12 KINDS"); a pale disc 240 across with the material's sign at 78; the name at 44 Black (reduced to fit), "×count of 999" (or NONE YET) and "Worth N each" in ExtraBold Muted; a rule; the description in Bold 28 and, under it, where it comes from in ExtraBold Muted 26 ("Left behind by Fire-type Pokémon.", "Now and then left behind by…", "Found out in the field."). Its hints: up and down to move, Esc to go back.
- **The kit's TMS tab** (`ModernUi.DrawMachines`, plan 39 · CR4): a second tab, TMS (Blue, the disc's sign), shown only when the kit is opened at a PC (`craft tm`), where it is the tab the kit opens on. **The list** has a row for every TM and TR the player has ever carried, TMs by number and then TRs, in the same rows as the materials': a disc 52 across at the left in its move's type colour with a rim darker by a quarter (a washed colour, Lighter by 0.55, while the machine can't be made now), "TM27  Return" at 32 Black and ×how many are in the bag at the right; a machine that can't be made now (a material or the money short, the bag full) is dimmed like a material not held. With none to list the panel says so in Muted 32. **The detail panel**: MONEY in the small Muted label at the upper left and the money held at the upper right (our mark and amount, 30 Black); a disc 200 across in the type's colour with the machine's name in white 40 Black; the move's name at 44 Black (reduced to fit); the type's pill and, beside it, "Power N", "Power varies" (a damaging move whose data gives no power) or "No damage" in ExtraBold Muted 26; a rule; IT TAKES in the small label, and a line for each need: its material's sign at 26 and name at 30 ExtraBold, and held / needed at the right in Black, green when held enough and red when short, then the price in money, green when the purse reaches it and red when not. Last, the words of the last press ("Made TM27, Return!" in green, or why it can't be made in red) wrap at 28 ExtraBold. Its hints: up and down to move, left and right for the tab, A to make, Esc to go back.
- **The kit's MAKE tab** (`ModernUi.DrawRecipes`, plan 39 · CR3): a tab after MATERIALS, MAKE (Green, the tools' sign), shown wherever the kit is opened. **The list** has a row for every recipe of `recipes.json`'s `kit`, in the table's order, in the same rows as the materials': a disc 52 across at the left in the colour of what it makes (a ball's Red, medicine's Green, anything else Gold) with a rim darker by a quarter (washed, Lighter by 0.55, while it can't be made now), the item's name at 32 Black and ×how many of it the bag holds at the right; a recipe that can't be made now (something short, the bag full) is dimmed like a material not held. **The detail panel**: MAKES in the small Muted label at the upper left; a disc 200 across in the row's colour with ×how many it makes each time in white Black, 40 reduced to fit 150 across (the words reached the rim at 40); the item's name at 44 Black (reduced to fit); a rule; IT TAKES in the small label and a line for each need as the TMS tab draws them (a material with its sign at 26, an item with a Frame disc 26 across), held / needed at the right in Black, green when held enough and red when short. While "how many?" is asked each need reads held / needed times the number, and under them stands the Stepper (the pill 240 by 84 in Frame, the number in white 44 Black, the arrows in Selection) with "How many?" over it in ExtraBold Muted 26. Last, the words of the last press ("Made Great Ball ×3!" in green, or why not in red) wrap at 28 ExtraBold. Its hints: up and down to move, left and right for the tab, A to make; while the number is asked, up and down by one, left and right by ten, A to make that many, Esc to put it away.
- **The kit's PANTRY tab** (`ModernUi.DrawPantry`, plan 39 · CR6): shown only when the kit is opened at the workshop's counter (`craft pantry`), where it is the tab the kit opens on: PANTRY in the pantry's green (`110,168,88`), the pouch's sign. **The list** has a row for every good of the pantry in the materials' rows (its sign, its name, ×how many the pouch holds), never dimmed; **the detail panel** has MONEY and the purse at the upper right as the TMS tab has them, the disc 240 across with the sign at 78, the name at 44 Black, "N each" in ExtraBold Muted 26, a rule and the description in Bold 28, and then the Stepper and the last press's words as MAKE has them. Its hints: up and down to move, left and right for the tab, A to buy (and again to buy that many), Esc to go back.
- **The kit's CROSSES page** (`ModernUi.DrawCrosses`, plan 39 · CR5): the last tab wherever the kit is opened, CROSSES in a berry's pink (`214,92,128`) with the berry sign. **The list** has a row for every row of `recipes.json`'s `crosses`, in the table's order, in the materials' rows: a berry's chip at the left (a disc in the pink with a rim darker by a half, a pale highlight, a short stalk and a green leaf; washed to `206,178,186` with a pale leaf until found), the berry's name at 32 Black once crossing has given it and "?????" until then (dimmed), and FOUND at the right once it has. **The detail panel**: CROSSES in the small Muted label at the upper left and "found / of all" as a Frame tag at the upper right; a disc 240 across with the chip at 78; the name (or "?????") at 44 Black; a rule; the pair it comes from in Bold 28 ("Two different Berries whose strongest flavour is spicy, planted side by side.", or "A Liechi Berry planted beside a Berry whose strongest flavour is sweet."), shown whether found or not, and under it how crossing works in ExtraBold Muted 26. Its hints: up and down to move, left and right for the tab, Esc to go back.
- **The pasture** (plan 35 · BN5, BN6; `Data/maps/SinnohPasture.json` and its copies): not a screen but a meadow of the field, a hand-made outdoor map 26 by 20 drawn with the field's own tiles. Trees round its edge, a rock face with a cave's mouth in its top left (CaveWall, CaveMouth, a path of dirt before it), a pond at the top right, a row of seven orchard trees across the left (row 8), the hot spring's pool of three by two in sand at the left, a lawn of flowers in the middle, a green-roofed shed (the Farm architecture) at the right with the board's signpost before it, a fence along row 16 with the gate's path through it at the middle of the south edge, and the keeper (the Rancher look) beside the gate. **The herd** stands on the lawn north of row 15 as Pokémon of the map (their field sprites, a Poké Ball standing in until baked), each wandering two tiles round its own tile; one spoken to shows its mood's bubble over its head for 1.2 s (the follower's bubbles by mood). **At work** (BN6): the orchard's pickers stand still under the trees on row 9 facing them, the hot spring's bathers in its pool to the chest (wading 10 texels, as the Resort Area's swimmers). The PC's question before its menu is a `choose` of three (USE THE PC, GO TO THE PASTURE, SWITCH OFF), and of four with the Stroll Counter in the bag (STROLL COUNTER before SWITCH OFF). The board's CHOOSE A BOX opens the PC's own boxes titled WHICH BOX GRAZES?, the cursor held on the box's name, with the hints Change box, Pick and Back (and WHO GOES FOR A STROLL? over the box's places when the counter picks a Pokémon). Owed: a basket of Eggs by the shed for the nursery, a cart at the cave's mouth, and the shots.
- **Poké Jobs** (`JobsScreen`, `ModernUi.Jobs.cs`, plan 15 · E3): titled POKé JOBS, opened from a PC's POKé JOBS in a game played by the modern rules; the requests' frame, a list panel 1000 wide on the left and a detail panel beside it. **The board** lists the six places of the board (three before plan 35 · BN6) as list rows (34 Black, a name from 104 with the menu icon on its disc): a Pokémon away (its name, its job's word in its colour as the row's value, or BACK in Green once its hours are over) or an empty place (POST A JOB, Muted, the icon's disc empty with a plus in Rule). The detail of a Pokémon away: its sprite at 2× on a ball disc of 150, its name in 44 Black and Lv., the job on a tag of its colour (ERRAND Blue, ORCHARD Green, HOT SPRING Red, NURSERY Gold, CAVE in Track's slate, the last two plan 35 · BN6's, with a tent and a star for their chips), then HOURS and DUE BACK (the time left, or BACK) as captions over values, and a bar 20 tall in the job's colour of the hours done over the hours asked, with "3 of 8 hours" under it; A brings it back. The detail of an empty place says in 30 Bold Muted what a job is for. **Choosing**: the list shows the boxes' Pokémon (Eggs too, since BN6: the nursery keeps them) as list rows with Lv. at the right; the detail shows the one under the cursor as the board shows one away. **Setting off**: the detail panel turns into the job's choice: a strip of `Tabs` for the kinds it may be given (an Egg the nursery alone; anyone else the errand, the orchard, the hot spring and the cave), under it the hours on a `Stepper` (1 to 24, "8 h"), the row with the cursor marked by a Selection ring at its left, and under them what the job pays an hour in 26 ExtraBold Muted lines (EXP, the effort's stat, friendship, berries); Up / Down choose the row, Left / Right change it, A sends the Pokémon off. **Back** (the result): a panel 1100 wide in the middle over the dimmed board, the Pokémon at 2× on a disc of 110, "Bidoof is back!" in 48 Black, then a line for each thing paid in 30 ExtraBold Ink (the hours paid for, EXP and the level reached, a move learned, effort, friendship, each berry, treat and find and how many, and for the nursery's Egg "The Egg is warmer and nearer hatching." or "The Egg is about to hatch!", whose hatching plays as the board closes), A to go on. Hints: Z Choose (Bring back on a Pokémon away), Esc Back.
- **The Global Link counter** (`GlobalLinkScreen`, `ModernUi.GlobalLink.cs`, plan 07 · O5, O7): titled GLOBAL LINK, opened by the Union Room desk upstairs in every Pokémon Center once an online server is named in the options file; the requests' frame. **The menu**: a list panel 1000 wide on the left with three name-only rows (MAKE A CODE, ENTER A CODE, LEAVE; names from 48, no icon disc), a Rule line under them and the session's line in 30 ExtraBold Ink; the detail panel beside it holds YOUR FRIEND CODE as a caption over the code in 52 Black (`1234-5678-9012`), SERVER over the server's host and port in 32 (never a path), and what the counter is for in 26 ExtraBold Muted. **The digits**: the list panel holds a keypad of twelve keys, three a row, 180 wide and 144 tall with 24 between (1 to 9, then ERASE, 0 and OK; a digit in Black 64, a word in Black 34; the key under the cursor a Selection pill, the rest Disc), and the detail panel YOUR FRIEND'S CODE over eight boxes of 76 by 91 in two groups of four (a gap of 28 between the groups), each digit typed in Black 60, the next box marked by a Selection bar along its foot, the line under them. **Waiting** (connecting, a code asked for, made, or being joined, and a failure): one panel 1280 wide in the middle; a code made is YOUR CODE over the code in Black 110 in two groups of four, with "Works for 9:42 more" in 30 Muted, counted down in the game's own time; then the line in Black 34 (Red for a failure) and a refusal's details in 24 ExtraBold Muted. **The room**: the list panel holds the team then the boxes as list rows (the menu icon on its disc, Lv. at the right, OFFERED in Selection on the one offered); the detail panel holds two halves, YOU OFFER and "{name} OFFERS" captions each over the Pokémon's sprite at 1× on a ball disc of 100, its name in Black 40, Lv. in 28 Muted, its OT and what it holds in 26 ExtraBold Muted, and READY TO TRADE in Green 28 once that side has agreed ("Nothing yet" in 32 Muted before an offer); under them a Rule, the other's friend code in 24 Black Muted, the line in 28 ExtraBold Ink, and a refused Pokémon's faults in 22 Muted. A on a Pokémon offers it, A on the one offered (both offered) agrees to trade; Esc calls the trade off, or leaves the room with no offer. **Traded**: a panel 1100 wide in the middle over the dimmed room, the Pokémon received at 2× on a disc of 130 and the line in Black 34 beside it; A goes back to the room. **The battle room** (plan 07 · O6): the menu's third row is MAKE A BATTLE CODE (so four rows, LEAVE last); a battle code is made, shown and typed as a trade's, and once both are in, the list panel holds YOUR TEAM as a caption over the team as list rows (icon on its disc, name, Lv. at the right), and the detail panel YOU BATTLE over the other's name in Black 64, their friend code in 24 Black Muted under it, the room's rules ("FLAT 50 · SIX AGAINST SIX · ONE OF EACH SPECIES AND ITEM") in 24 Black Muted, then the line in 30 ExtraBold Ink and a refused team's faults in 22 Muted. A brings the team as it stands; while it is checked and the other's awaited the waiting panel says so; once both are greeted the counter closes to black and opens on the battle (indoors), which closes back onto the counter. **After the battle**: the waiting panel's 1280 in the middle, the line saying how it went in Black 34 ("You beat Dawn! The win was written into your record.") and under it "With Dawn: 1 won, 0 lost, 0 drawn" in 30 Black Muted; A goes back to the menu. Hints by the place: Z Choose / Type / Offer / Trade / Bring this team, Esc Back / Erase / Call off / Leave.
- **Credits** (`TitleCreditsScreen`, `ModernUi.TitleCredits.cs`, plan 16 · T1): titled CREDITS, opened from the title's CREDITS row; whose work is in the game, not the roll after the Hall of Fame (that is "The credits"). One panel the whole content width (`Margin` to `Margin`, `ContentTop` to `ContentBottom`), rising 60 into place over 0.3 s, with the scroll bar at its right edge. Its text is wrapped once as the screen opens and drawn a row at a time on a pitch of 42, 44 in from the panel's left: a section's heading in 36 Black (Ink; the first section's in Blue), its lines in 28 ExtraBold Ink, and an empty row between sections. **The first section is the project's own** ("This fan remake": that the art, sound and words are made for it or generated by its code and nothing is taken from the games, that the code is under the MIT licence, and the fan-game notice), in the language of the moment; then a section for each pack in use, in load order (plan 16 · T15: "Pack: name version" as its heading, then the credit its manifest carries, who made it and what it is, as the pack writes them, and the line that a pack is its maker's own work); after them come the sections of `docs/art/CREDITS.md` and `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md` as the files have them, in English, read as plain text (a table's row as "Header: value" pairs joined by " · ", a licence's text as its paragraphs). Up and down scroll a row; left and right (and Q and E) go to the heading of the section before or after; B goes back. Hints: Up / Down Scroll, Left / Right Section, Esc Back.
- **Packs** (`PacksScreen`, `ModernUi.Packs.cs`, plan 16 · T15): opened from the options' PACKS row (its value "n in use"), titled PACKS, over the options as the Controls page is. A panel of list rows, four at a time with the scroll bar past them: each pack's place in the order in Muted 28 at the row's left, its name as a list row's name (Muted when it is off), its version ("Version 1.0", ExtraBold 28) and a tag ON in Green or OFF in Muted at the right. Under it a panel of the pack under the cursor: MADE BY and the author (34 Black; "Not said" when the manifest has none), LOADS with a Stepper "2 / 3" at the right, CREDITS with the credit wrapped in ExtraBold 28, WHAT IT IS with its description in Muted, and "Loads after …" at the foot when its manifest names packs. A note panel 84 tall at the foot says, in Red, that the changes take hold the next time the game starts while what would load differs from what is in use (the data is read once, before anything else), else how many packs couldn't be read and the first one's fault in Red, else a line in Muted on what a pack is. When both hold the note says both, the wait first and the fault on the line under it (a pack left out is never hidden by a change waiting). The parts of the pack's panel follow one another down it, so a credit of three lines pushes WHAT IT IS down rather than writing over it, and a description stops a line above "Loads after …". A turns the pack on or off, Left and Right move it in the order, B goes back; each change is written to the options at once. With no pack found it says so and names the folders packs are looked for in, each a line, a folder too long for the panel shortened in its middle with an ellipsis so that its drive and its last folders show.
- **Migrate** (`MigrateScreen`, `ModernUi.Migrate.cs`, plan 08 · P21): the title's MIGRATE, titled MIGRATE; it brings six Pokémon out of the player's own files into a save's Pal Park. It has four steps, each two panels like the Vs. Recorder's. **Which save**, only when there are several: the saves as list rows (the player's name, SLOT n as the value), and on the right a tag (NATIONAL POKÉDEX Green, NO NATIONAL POKÉDEX Red) and what waits in the park. **Which file**: the files of the folder as list rows (the name, GBA SAVE, PLATINUM SAVE, POKÉMON FILE or n POKÉMON FILES as the value; a file the readers refuse is CAN'T READ in Red; a file that can't give now is a Muted row); on the right the file's kind, its trainer, how many Pokémon its boxes hold and a tag, READY Green or NOT NOW Red with the reason in Bold 30 (the National Pokédex, fewer than six, six still waiting, a day to wait with its hours); a file the readers refuse says why. **A file's boxes**, drawn as the PC draws one: a panel 792 wide with the box's name on its plate between its arrows (Q and E change the box) and its number under it, six places by five of 112, each a Pokémon's icon; the one under the cursor has the Selection edge, a Pokémon that can't come is a grey icon with a small Red "!" at its corner, and one chosen is a Gold edge on a cream cell with its number, 1 to 6, in a Gold disc at the corner. Under the places a tag counts n / 6 CHOSEN (Green at six). On the right the Pokémon under the cursor: its icon at 4× on a disc, its name (the nickname, else the species), level and species, its original trainer, and a tag CAN COME Green, CHOSEN Green or CAN'T COME Red with the reason below it (an Egg; a move of the older games the park won't take; a key item; no such species; it has come before; or what the legality check found, such as a move it can't learn); along the foot THE SIX, six discs filled with the icons in the order chosen. The Menu button asks, with six, "Bring these 6 over?" in the prompt's panel with NO, WAIT (Blue, highlighted) and YES, BRING THEM (Green); a notice follows, "They are on their way" or the reason nothing was brought, with one OK. Nothing in the folder is written, whatever is answered.

  **From this game's other saves, and the way out** (plan 08 · P22). From the title the screen opens on **what to do**, two list rows (BRING POKÉMON OVER, WRITE A POKÉMON OUT), and on the right a line in Bold 30 of what the highlighted one does. **Bring** goes on as above, and the game's other saves are among the files, each a row with the player's name and THIS GAME · SLOT n as its value (the save the Pokémon go to is not offered, since it has no Pokémon to give itself); its boxes are drawn as a file's are, and on the right a Pokémon refused for being in the receiving save already, for an Egg or for what the legality check found says so. A move between saves is final, so the question says that the six leave the other save ("They will wait in Pal Park… and leave the save they are in"). **Write out** asks which save when there are several, then shows the save's team first (a box named TEAM, of six places) and its boxes after it, one Pokémon to choose: every Pokémon can be chosen, so the right-hand panel shows it as above without a verdict, and under it a tag in Bold 30, AS A FILE OR AS TEXT Green, or AS TEXT ONLY Gold with a line of why (a species a `.pk4` can't hold). A prompt in the same panel asks **Write {name} out as** with A .PK4 FILE (Blue; Muted when it can't be) and SHOWDOWN TEXT (Green); a notice follows, "Written out" with the name of the file and that it is in the out folder, or "Nothing was written" with one OK. Nothing leaves the save.
- **Home** (`HomeScreen`, `ModernUi.Home.cs`, plan 12 · Q28, Q29): the title's HOME, titled HOME. It opens on **what to do**, two panels like Migrate's: TRADE BETWEEN SAVES (a Muted row with one save, whose right panel says a trade needs two) and THE HOME BOX, and on the right the row's name in Black 40 over a rule and what it does in Bold 30, with the Home box's count; a Home box that can't be opened says why in Red under it. **Which save**, only when there is a choice (a trade with three saves, the Home box with two or three): the saves as list rows, the player's name and SLOT n as the value, and on the right the question ("Trade from which save?", "And with which save?", "Open the Home box beside which save?"). **The two sides**: two panels of equal width side by side across the content area, each with its name in Black 32 at its top (the player and SLOT n for a save, HOME BOX n / 240 for the Home box), the side the cursor is on framed in Blue with its name in Blue; under the name list rows, each a Pokémon's icon on its disc, its name (the nickname, else the species) and as the value TEAM or BOX n and its level in Black 24, the team first and then the boxes in order; a Pokémon put in from a randomised run carries RANDOM before its value. Left and right change sides, up and down move in a list. In a trade the Pokémon chosen on a side is a Gold edge round its row; with one chosen on each the prompt asks "Trade these two?" with NO (Blue, highlighted) and YES, TRADE (Green). In the Home box A on a save's Pokémon puts it in and on the Home box's takes it out, at once. While the files are written the prompt's panel says "Writing the saves" with no buttons; then a notice, Traded, Put in the Home box or Taken out of the Home box with one OK, or "That can't be done" and the reason (the run under way, the last able Pokémon, no room, this very Pokémon already on the other side, an Egg, a battle suspended, a Home box of another version of the data). A refusal is said as the Pokémon is chosen, never after a question.
- **Starter choice** (`StarterSelectScreen`): the three partners as three cards side by side, each with its sprite at 3× on a disc, name, category and type; the chosen one hops. A panel under them has the chosen one's entry. Choosing asks first ("Choose Turtwig?"), with going back as the first answer.
- **Saving** (`SaveScreen`): over the dimmed field, the save as it will be (the same summary the title's CONTINUE card shows: place, player, time played, Pokédex, badges, party) with the question under it. SAVE is the first answer and the one highlighted, since nothing is lost by saving. Afterwards the panel says so for a moment and goes. The panel names the slot the game is saved in (plan 12 · Q2), which is the one it was continued from or the empty one a new game took: as a small tag after the heading, as on the title's card, and in the question ("Save the game in slot 2?").
- **Options** (`OptionsScreen`): **eight tabs** across the top (2026-11-07; the kit's `Tabs`, 76 tall, 16 above the rows): GAME (gold, a star), FIELD (green, a map), BATTLE (red, a ball), DISPLAY (blue), SOUND (violet, a radio), CONTROLS (slate), ACCESS (teal) and MORE (coral), each holding its rows in the enum's order (`OptionsScreen.CategoryOf`); Q / E or the shoulder buttons turn them, a click on a tab shows it, and a tab opens on its first row. Under the tabs one card per setting, 78 tall on a pitch of 86 (eight rows under the tabs, the longest tabs scroll), with its value on a pill between two arrows; a panel under them, filling what is left of the screen, explains the chosen one. The first row is the language (plan 17 · N1): its value pill shows the language's own name for itself (English, Français), and only the languages whose script the interface's faces can draw are offered (Latin until plan 17 · N5), never the pseudo-language that brackets every string for the tests. The second row is the text speed (slow, normal, fast: 24, 45 and 120 characters a second, whatever the frame rate). The last three are the sound: on or off (the M key too), then the music's volume and the sound effects', 0 to 100 in steps of 10, shown as a percentage, which stop at their ends rather than wrapping round. A setting the newest games made their own default (plan 12) is ON or OFF, ON unless chosen: since plan 06 · R19 every game is played by the modern rules, so the RULES it once had between the two is gone, and an older settings file's RULES reads as ON. The last row is HELP, whose value is OPEN: the A button opens the help pages. Its help line under the rows ends by naming the build, as the title's corner does ("This is version 0.1.0.": plan 16 · T2). Just before it come the two rows of plan 16 · T3: **Pause in the background** (OFF or ON, off unless chosen: on, the game waits where it is and its sound drops to a quarter while another window is in front, and goes on when the player comes back) and **Frame limit** (30 FPS, 60 FPS, 120 FPS or NO LIMIT, 60 unless chosen: the most frames drawn a second and nothing more, beside V-Sync, which still holds the game to the display's refresh rate when it is on). After the Battle Style, just before HELP, come the three rows of plan 12 · Q4: **Button mode** (NORMAL, START = X or L = A, normal unless chosen: Platinum's own three, presets of the pad's bindings; the help line under the row says which buttons change places), **Stick dead zone** (10% to 60%, 35% unless chosen: how far the pad's left stick is pushed before it walks or moves a cursor; the value is a percentage on the pill and stops at its ends) and **Controller** (PAD 1 to PAD 4, the first unless chosen: which connected pad is read; the keyboard works whichever it is). After Record battles, before CONTROLS, comes **Slot 2 game** (plan 08 · P23: NONE, RUBY, SAPPHIRE, EMERALD, FIRERED or LEAFGREEN, none unless chosen; the help line under it says that, with the National Pokédex, two of that game's Pokémon are met in the grass of the routes that have them, as if its cartridge were in the console's second slot). The last setting before CONTROLS is **Season** (plan 22 · U3: CALENDAR, SPRING, SUMMER, AUTUMN or WINTER, the calendar unless chosen; the help line says a season is Unova's alone and, on the calendar, which month brings which). **Autosave** (plan 12 · Q3: OFF or ON, off unless chosen) comes after Boxes anywhere, before CONTROLS; the disc it shows is under "Notices". A new row goes at the end, before HELP. **Reset to defaults** (plan 12 · Q3, value RESET) is the very last row, after HELP, and the A button asks "Reset every option?" in the kit's prompt (the screen dimmed, the panel rising 60 into place): NO, KEEP THEM in Blue first and highlighted, YES, RESET in Red; B is NO. Yes puts every option back as a new game has it (the language, sound, graphics and controls too) and keeps the scenes the machine has seen.
- **Help** (`HelpScreen`, plan 12 · Q10): three tabs, TYPES (Red), CONTROLS (Blue) and NOTES (Green). TYPES is the type chart as a grid on a panel at the left: the attacking types down its side as type pills 150 wide, the defending ones across its top as pills of their three-letter short names, and in each cell the matchup as a signed pill 30 tall, the sign as the battle's hint pills have it with ×2 (Green), ×½ (Gold) or ×0 (Muted), a plain matchup left as a faint dot. The cursor's cell is ringed in the Selection colour, its row and column lit faintly, and a panel at the right says it in words ("Fire against Grass: super effective, twice the damage") with the three pills explained under it. The chart is the one the game is played by (`TypeChart.GetEffectiveness` under the game's rules). CONTROLS lists every action the game reads as a list row with its keys and the pad's buttons (the preset of the options' Button mode, a player's own bindings over it) on Frame-coloured key pills; full screen and the sound are rows of their own now (the Fullscreen and Mute actions), not a footnote. NOTES is a few short paragraphs of our own on what helps a new player (running, the Pokétch, saving, the options), in Bold 30 on 42 leading. On the chart the arrows move the cursor, and up from its top row goes to the tabs; on the tabs (and on the other two pages) left and right change tab and down goes into the page. B goes back to the options. **TIPS** (plan 12 · Q23), a fourth tab after NOTES, Gold with the kit's star: the trainer tips this game has shown, in the order the game lists them, as list rows (84 high on a pitch of 92, eight to the panel, scrolling with the kit's bar) on a panel 640 wide at the left, the row under the cursor a Selection pill; the panel at the right shows that tip as the card has it, its title in 44 Black Ink and its lines in 30 ExtraBold Ink on 42 leading. Up and Down move the cursor (up from the first row goes to the tabs, down from the tabs into the list). A game that has shown none says so in the middle of the one panel, in 30 ExtraBold Muted ("No tips yet. They come as you play, while the options' TIPS row is on."). The tab is there with the row off too, so what was shown can still be read.
- **The first start** (`FirstStartScreen`, plan 16 · T24): a page of a few of the options' rows, drawn as the options draw them, before the title on the first run; its layout is under "Opening and title screen", "The first start".
- **Controls** (`ControlsScreen`, plan 12 · Q5; the options' row CONTROLS, before HELP): the title at the top left, the hints of the moment at the top right, a panel with a header of the two columns, KEYBOARD at x 520 and CONTROLLER at x 1180 from the panel's edge, in the section-label style, and under it eleven rows 62 apart and 56 tall that scroll with a thin track at the panel's right edge: an action to a row (its name in Black 30, **Red when another action shares its key or button**, a Blue dot before it when it differs from the Button mode's own), its keys as Frame pills (the same pills as the help page) in the first column and its buttons as signs (`PadGlyphArt` at 0.9) in the second; "None" in Muted for a column with nothing. The cursor row is lit faintly (Selection at 46) and **the chosen cell is ringed** in Selection, 4 px wide (Red while waiting for a press, with an ellipsis in the place of the keys). The last row is a Selection pill, PUT EVERYTHING BACK. Under the panel a second panel says in words what is going on: the help line in Muted; "Press the new key for {action}. Esc keeps it as it was." (or the new button, which a pad's player can leave by waiting ten seconds); the clash in Red, "{input} already does {others}. Swap the two, or keep things as they were?", with A and B as Swap and Keep in the hints; the question of putting everything back; and the notice of what the last change did. Left and right move between the columns, A waits for the new input (the first key or button pressed becomes the action's first, at most three are kept; Esc cancels, and on the pad any button cancels a keyboard capture), the item button puts the action back as the Button mode has it, B goes back. A clash already in the file shows in Red without asking. Nothing may be left with no way to answer the game: a change that would leave Confirm, Cancel or Menu with neither key nor button is refused with a line saying so.
- **The Pokégear** (`PokegearScreen`, plan 20 · J3; Johto's alone): the menu screens' backdrop, its title at the top left and the clock at the top right on a dark pill (the weekday and the hour, Black 40 in white), the cards under them as `Tabs` 300 wide each (PHONE Green with a handset, MAP Blue with a folded map, RADIO Gold with a set and its aerial, only those held), and the card on show in one panel under them. The start menu's entry is the Pokégear's own teal (40 168 186) with a round handheld on its chip. **Phone**: the numbers as list rows on a panel 1060 wide, each with a Green handset on its disc, and beside it a card of the one under the cursor: the name in Black 44 over a rule, WHERE TO FIND THEM and the place, and for a trainer READY TO BATTLE AGAIN with the day and the time of day ("Mondays, in the day"), and a Selection tag WAITING FOR YOU while a rematch waits. **Map**: the region's picture (`ModernUi.RegionMap`, the Town Map's own) with the player's cell lit and ringed, and on the right MAP CARD, a line on what the A button opens, and YOU ARE IN with the place. **Radio**: a round dial 600 across, the Pokégear's teal darkened under three faint rings, a pale spot for each station received (Gold when tuned, its outer ring a soft disc), and the cursor a Selection ring 22 round a white dot; on the right the station's name in Black 44, a Muted FAINT tag when it comes through static and a Gold tag with the song it plays, and its words on a pale strip. The arrows move the cursor while held; Q and E (left and right on the other cards) change cards.
- **The PokéNav** (`PokeNavScreen`, plan 21 · H2, part b; Hoenn's alone): the Pokégear's frame with its own colour, the orange of its case (236 132 46), on its start menu entry's chip (a folded map), the handsets' discs and the condition's shape, and its pages as `Tabs` (HOENN MAP Blue, CONDITION Gold, MATCH CALL Green, RIBBONS Red; Match Call once added, Ribbons once a Pokémon wears one). **Map**: the region's picture as the Pokégear's map card has it, the player's cell lit and ringed, the A button opening the map whole. **Condition**: the Pokémon on a card 560 wide (its sprite at three times on a pale disc, its name, its place in the team), and beside it a pentagon of four rings, COOL at the top and on clockwise in the five qualities' own colours, the condition laid over it in the PokéNav's orange (its fill at 150 alpha, its edge solid), with each quality's number and the SHEEN beside it; up and down go through the team. **Match Call**: the Pokégear's phone list with the handsets on orange discs, and the card of the one under the cursor (the name, WHERE TO FIND THEM and the place). **Ribbons**: the team as list rows with each one's count of ribbons at the row's right, and the card of the one under the cursor.
- **The travel phone** (`PhoneScreen`, `ModernUi.Phone.cs`, plan 43 · LF6; every region whose file says `"phone": true`, Unova and the regions after it): the Pokégear's frame titled PHONE, in its region's colour (Unova's slate blue 70 110 170, Kalos's rose 214 92 128, any other's teal 60 150 140) on its handsets' discs; the start menu's entry is the teal with a handset on its chip, after the PokéNav's place. Its cards as `Tabs` 300 wide (CALLS Green with a handset, NEWS Gold with a letter, MAP Blue with a folded map, the map only where the region has one). **Calls**: the Pokégear's phone list and the card of the one under the cursor (the name in Black 44 over a rule, WHERE TO FIND THEM and the place, WAITING FOR YOU in Red Black 26 while a rematch waits). **News**: on a cream sheet (252 248 238) inset 40 in the panel, TODAY'S NEWS in Black 40 over a rule and the day's programme in ExtraBold 32, a line every 44 and 14 between its lines ("Nothing on the air today." in Muted when nothing is). **Map**: the Pokégear's map card, MAP for its heading. A call plays over the field as a Pokégear's does; the caller's face rendered on the call (as the bands' busts are) is owed.
- **The Ruins of Alph's panels** (`AlphPuzzleScreen`, plan 20 · J5): the menu screens' backdrop and title (STONE PANEL), and in the middle a slab of sandstone (150 128 98 to 118 98 74, a 6-unit rim of 92 76 58) six squares of 120 by six, its middle four by four sunk as the frame in the rim's colour, the ring's squares a shade darker than the slab and its corners plain. A piece is a tile of pale stone (214 194 158 to 186 164 128) with its sixteenth of the picture cut into it: the species' own menu sprite drawn in a dark brown (78 58 40), turned as the piece lies; nothing of the original's picture. The cursor is a white ring round its square; the piece in hand rides 18 units over it on a soft shadow. Whole, the tiles glow gold (255 232 160) for 1.6 seconds and the panel closes. The foot is a panel with what to do, ExtraBold 36.
- **The Bug-Catching Contest's results** (`BugContestResultsScreen`, plan 15 · E5): the menu screens' backdrop and title (BUG-CATCHING CONTEST), the hint at the top right (NEXT while places are left to tell, then CLOSE). A board 1180 wide at the content's top left with a row for each of the six, best first, each a list's row: its place (`1.`) at the left in Black 30, Gold for the three that win and Muted for the rest; who (their class and name, or the player's name) at 104; the species caught at 620 (or "Nothing caught"); the score right-aligned. The player's row is the selection's pill. A place not told yet is a bare rule across its row. Places are told third, second and first, one a press; those below third come with the first. Once first is told, its catch stands on a leaf-green disc (`92,168,84`, a paler disc inside it) 300 to the right of the board, growing in over 0.35 s, the sprite at twice its size, WINNER under it in white. Along the foot the notices' panel says the judges are at work, and once all is told the player's own score.

### The new-game introduction (`IntroScreen`)

The games' opening talk, in our own words and with our own pictures: Professor Rowan in front of a soft backdrop, speaking in the dialogue panel.

1. Black, then the backdrop fades up: deep teal to night blue with slow drifting discs of light, and Rowan (his 3D model, as battles show a trainer, breathing and blinking) in the middle.
2. He welcomes the player and explains the world. Then he steps aside, a Poké Ball (the 3D one of the battles) comes up and opens in white light, and a Pokémon grows out of the light, hops and stands beside him: Buneary, as in Platinum (the 3D model, as in battle).
3. **Who are you?** Two cards, a boy and a girl (the two player characters as 3D models); the chosen one stands a little forward and waves. Choosing asks whether that is right ("So you're a girl?").
   **What do you set out in?** (plan 11 · C10) One card of the same size in the middle (520 by 640, `ModernUi.IntroOutfitCard`), rising in as the two did: the look chosen as its 3D model, dressed in the outfit under the cursor and waving now and then, the outfit's name under it in capitals in the selection colour, a pip for each outfit offered (the one shown larger and in the selection colour), and a white arrow either side saying that left and right go round. The look's own clothes come first, so a press goes on as before; then the sets of `clothes.json` that suit the look. Rowan's question stays in the panel below, and B goes back to the two cards. An outfit not built yet shows the look's own clothes until it is (as the wardrobe does), so the cursor never waits. The chosen outfit is what the name entry's sprite and the send-off wear.
4. **Your name?** The name-entry screen: the character's field sprite at 6×, the name so far as large letters on a row of seven slots, and the keyboard. Seven letters at most, as in Platinum; an empty name takes the default (Lucas or Dawn).
5. He repeats the name and asks whether it is right. In both of his questions YES comes first and going back second: nothing is lost either way, and yes is what is nearly always meant.
6. The send-off: the picture closes to black round the player's own field sprite, which shrinks away in whole steps on a small pool of light (`UiShapes.Glow`), and the field fades in.

Any line can be hurried with the A button; nothing can be skipped, because each step sets something.

Whoever the player isn't is the professor's assistant, with the other default name. Written lines and place names never name the player outright: they say `{player}` and `{assistant}`, which `PlayerIdentity.Fill` replaces when they are shown.

### Pokémon in menus

- Always **2D pixel sprites** baked from the models (`PokemonSprites`): 48-px icons and 128-px front/back sprites with a one-texel outline, the eyes and markings baked in with them.
- Shown at whole-number scales only (party icons at 4×), point-filtered.
- Icons hop like the main games: the selected one 3 sprite-pixels every 0.16 s, the others 1 sprite-pixel every 0.4 s; fainted ones stay still.
- **A strip** (plan 09 · L5): where one Pokémon is looked at, its front sprite breathes. The strip is eight frames of the idle clip over one breath (1.7 s divided by the model's `Tempo`), baked like the stills (same light, framing, outline and 128 px frames side by side) and played by `FrameClock`, a frame every eighth of the breath, at the same whole-number scale as the still it replaces. Its first frame is the still itself, and its frames fold the breath's second half into its first (each frame the clip at its moment blended toward the clip a breath earlier, by its eighth), so the eighth frame leads back to the first with no jump. It plays in three places only: the Pokédex's INFO page, once the entry's one hop (3 sprite-pixels for 0.16 s as it opens) has landed; the summary's sprite; and the chosen card of the starter choice, which breathes in place of hopping. Everywhere else (lists, icons, boxes, the INFO page beside the list before the entry is opened, the FORMS and SIZE pages) keeps the still. Until a strip is baked the still stands in its place, unmoving (the chosen starter goes on hopping as before). At most four strips are held at once.
- **The care close-up** (plan 35 · BN2, `CareScreen`, `ModernUi.Care.cs`) is the second and last place a Pokémon is a 3D model on a screen of menus, a scene as the gallery's: the camp's clearing behind, dimmed to 60 %, and over it the Pokémon front on (the gallery's framing, `SpriteView.Turntable`, turned a half turn back to face the player), in a picture 900 units square centred at row 520, its target a GPU ledger's owner of its own (`care`), one model a frame and nothing in a headless game; the still sprite at 4× stands in until the model is built.
- **The gallery** (plan 12 · Q30) is the one place outside a battle, the evolution, the hatching and the title where a Pokémon is shown as its 3D model in a menu: a scene of its own, as the evolution's is, never a list's row. The model stands on the evolution's stage (its dark hall, pool of light and slow rays, without the glow), framed so that it fits at every turn (`SpriteView.Turntable`: the frame is the model's height and its widest reach from its middle in any direction), under the sprites' studio light with its outline, drawn smooth (it is no pixel art): the picture is 1120 units square, clipped to the stage, and the model's frame fills 680 of it at the first zoom, so a zoomed model is cut by the stage's edge and never by the picture's. It turns on its own at a quarter turn every two seconds until the player turns it by hand; front and back are a half turn apart.
