Its clips are the studio's eleven in its order (IDLE, PHYSICAL, SPECIAL, STATUS, HIT, FAINT, ENTRY, DODGE, TIRED, CHEER, CHARGE), a move's clip played over 1.2 s and held 0.6 s before it plays again; the zoom is three steps (1, 1.5 and 2 times, toward the frame's middle). Until its model is built the menu's still sprite stands in its place at a whole-number scale (4×), unturned.

- **A shiny Pokémon** (plan 09 · L1) is shown in its shiny colours wherever that Pokémon is shown (its look name, `Pokemon.LookName`), and its name has a **star** after it (after the gender mark, `UiIcons.ShinyStar`, `UiIcon.Star`): five points, gold (240, 176, 40) with a darker rim (176, 112, 20) a tenth of its size wide, as tall as 0.7 of the name's size, in the battle's HP boxes, the team's cards, the summary, the PC and wherever else the name is drawn with its gender. A species' Pokédex entry shows a shiny card on its FORMS page, after the looks seen, once one of the species has been caught shiny (`Pokedex.CaughtShiny`).

### Eggs

An Egg (plan 06 · R15) is our own: a cream shell (246, 236, 210) shaded in a crescent along its lower right (222, 206, 172), whose edge follows the outline, five mint-green spots (104, 178, 136) and a white shine on its upper left, an egg's shape (an ellipse narrowed toward its top), standing on the floor of its canvas.

- In menus it is pixel art like the baked sprites (`PixelArtGenerator.EggArt`): a 48-px icon and a 128-px sprite with the same one-texel outline, at the same whole-number scales. It rocks once every 0.8 s by one sprite-pixel instead of hopping, and is never dimmed for being unable to fight.
- On the team's card it shows its name, Egg, with no gender, level, types or HP, and a muted "Waiting to hatch". In battle's lists it shows its name alone. Its summary has the left panel (the Egg on its disc, "A Pokémon Egg", who it was received from and on what day) and on the right THE EGG WATCH: one of four sentences by the cycles left (five or fewer, ten, forty, more). The PC's detail column shows the same sentence under its name.
- The Pokétch's apps show its icon in the LCD's tones, with no HP bar.

### Hatching

An Egg hatches on the evolution scene's stage (`EvolutionStage`): the dark hall, its pool of light and the slow rays, the glow rising as the Egg breaks.

- The Egg is drawn large and smooth (`ModernUi.HatchEgg`): the menus' shell, spots and shine as vector shapes, 400 units tall, standing on the pool of light with a soft shadow under it, outlined in ink.
- It rocks three times about its foot over 3.2 s, each swing quick and dying away, each stronger than the last (0.10, 0.16, 0.22 radians), with the ball's wobble sound as each comes back to rest.
- Then for 1.5 s it shakes fast while ink cracks run across its upper half from left to right, and a second crack branches up from the middle; it swells a little and turns white at the end.
- A white flash fills the screen as it breaks (0.55 s): the Pokémon is there, drawn as the evolution scene draws its new shape, in white, which drains from it over 1.2 s as it cries.
- "X hatched from the Egg!" in the dialogue panel, with the fanfare of a Pokémon received. No nickname is asked, as none is when one is caught.

### The Hall of Fame's ceremony (`HallOfFameCeremony`)

Plan 02 · S14. The team's entry into the Hall of Fame is a scene of its own, on the beats of the original's (each Pokémon in turn, then the Champion among them) and wholly our own to look at.

- **The stage** (`ModernUi.CeremonyStage`) is the evolution scene's dark hall in the Hall of Fame's colours: night blue `10,12,34` at the top falling to a deep wine `58,18,34` at the foot, a pool of warm light on the floor (gold `255,214,130`, never white), and two spotlights from the top corners that sweep slowly across the picture and cross over whoever stands on the pool. No rays turn behind the figure: this is a hall, not a change.
- **Each Pokémon in turn**: its model, 640 units across, slides in from the left edge onto the pool, left of the middle, over 0.6 s (ease out), and cries as it comes to rest. Its card slides in from the right at the same time (`Panel`, 760 by 470): a caption in gold capitals, "WELCOME TO THE HALL OF FAME", which is on the card as it comes; its name at 64 with its gender's mark and its level at the card's right; its species and its types' pills; under a rule, who first trained it with their ID number, and where and at what level it was met. The card's three parts appear 0.4 s apart. A small count ("2 / 6") sits in the card's lower right. After 3.4 s, or at the button once the card is whole, both slide out the way they came (0.45 s) and the next comes.
- **The Champion among them**: the player's own figure (dressed as they are, 600 units tall) steps onto the pool in the middle; the team comes in from both edges of the picture to stand either side, the first on the right, the second on the left and so outward, each 300 units across and 0.18 s after the one before. Above them "LEAGUE CHAMPION" in the title's display lettering, gold `255,236,170` to `226,160,60`, with "Congratulations!" under it in cream; below them a dark pill with the Champion's name, ID number and the time played. Then slips of paper fall for as long as the picture stays: ninety, in gold, red, blue, green and cream, six units by fourteen, each turning as it falls at 140 to 260 units a second with a slow sway. Nothing is read from chance: a slip's place is a function of its number and the clock.
- The picture waits for the button (the red arrow, after three seconds), then closes to black over a second.
- **Don't** show stats, moves or a level bar: the card says who the Pokémon is and where it came from, as the original's does.

### The credits (`CreditsScreen`)

Plan 02 · S14. This remake's own credits: its sources and its thanks, in its own words (`Data/credits.json`; nothing of the original's roll, its scenes or its list of names).

- **The backdrop** (`ModernUi.CreditsBackdrop`) is the same night hall without the wine: `8,10,30` to `22,30,78`, with slow motes of soft light drifting upward (forty, blue-white, 0.06 to 0.16 alpha). It is quiet on purpose: text is the subject.
- **The title card** opens the roll: the title screen's own lettering ("Pokémon" in cream over "PLATINUM" in the silver gradient, with its rule and "A FAN REMAKE"), centred, fading in over 1.2 s, held, and fading out over 0.8 s; five seconds in all.
- **The roll** is one column, 860 units wide, centred at 0.64 of the picture's width. A section's heading is gold `240,176,56` capitals at 28, Black, with a short rule under it; a name is white at 38, ExtraBold; a note under a name is pale blue `178,196,236` at 24, Bold, wrapped to the column in lines of about the same width (`ModernUi.WrapEvenly`: never one word under a full line). Lines stand 52 apart (a note's lines 34), sections 110. It climbs at 64 units a second, four times as fast while the button is held, and fades over the last 150 units at the top and at the foot.
- **The team walks the player through it**: on the left, at 0.25 of the width, the team's Pokémon stand in turn on a pool of light, each for an equal share of the roll, as 3D models 520 units across, one fading into the next over 0.8 s, with its name on a dark pill under it. With no team (a tool's run) the pool is left empty.
- **The last card**: "THE END" in the display lettering, silver, with "Thank you for playing." under it in cream, fading in over 1.2 s. It waits for the button (the red arrow after 1.5 s) and goes on by itself after thirty seconds; then black over a second.
- A character outside Latin-1 never appears in the file (`UiFonts`), and a long dash is never typed: a middle dot separates.

### The Underground

Plan 06 · R16a. Everything below ground is our own: nothing of the original's lower-screen art, its trap pictures or its goods' models is taken.

- **The tunnels** are a cave like any other (`"setting": "Cave"`): the caves' rock and floor, the cave's own light rig that ignores the hour, and the caves' camera. A way into a secret base is a cave's mouth in the wall (`TileType.CaveMouth`), opened at the floor's height while a base lies behind it.
- **A way down to a hideaway** (plan 38 · EX6, two in each of the six tunnel systems) is a cave's mouth too, in the back wall over a passage, at the floor's height: the same dark hollow as a base's way in, so nothing new is drawn. Until Sinnoh's Hall of Fame it is the tunnels' rock like the rest (`Hideaways.LayWaysDown`), and nothing marks where it will open.
- **The radar's pulse** (`FieldLife.Glint`): what answers within six steps shows for a second and a half as a four-pointed star blinking over its tile (a cell of the life atlas, 16 texels), three rings spreading under it one after another and sparks hopping off it, in the colour of what it is: gold (255, 232, 130) for a wall that holds something, red (255, 110, 96) for a trap, blue (130, 200, 255) for a buried Sphere. Nothing else marks them: a wall that holds something looks like any other until the radar finds it.
- **The wall's game** (`ModernUi.DrawMining`) is a menu screen: the wall on a standard panel at the left, thirteen squares by ten, the tools and what the wall holds on two panels at the right, the line of text along the bottom.
  - Bedrock is (58, 50, 60). Dirt is drawn a square at a time in three tones by how thick it lies: two layers (186, 150, 104), four (140, 110, 80), six (104, 92, 92), each with a lighter top edge, a darker bottom edge and one to three stones at places of the square's own. Dirt hides whatever is under it until the square is bare.
  - A Sphere is a shaded ball in its colour (`SphereBall`: Prism (236, 232, 250) with two faint rings of colour, Pale (196, 178, 224), Red (226, 84, 76), Blue (78, 132, 226), Green (84, 184, 110)); any other thing is its shape in the wall's squares, flat, in a tone by what it is (`TreasureColour`: bone for a fossil, amber, the colour of a shard, a stone or a plate), lit from the upper left with a darker rim on its own edges. Dark rock is (84, 86, 104). A thing wholly bare has a white star pulsing on it.
  - The crack runs along a dark strip over the wall from the left as the wall weakens, a zigzag of ink five units thick; the strip's right end says THE WALL HOLDS or IT GAVE WAY and the crack stops short of it. A blow shakes the wall by a few units, harder and longer the nearer it is to falling in.
  - The cursor is a square's outline in the selection colour. The two tools are cards: the pick's selected card is blue, the hammer's red. IN THE WALL lists a row for each thing: "Something..." on a muted row until it is bare, then its picture and name on a pale green one.
- **A trap gone off** (`ModernUi.DrawTrap`) is drawn over the field in the console's own 256 by 192 dots stretched to the screen, with what to do in a pill at the bottom and the trap's name in a notice at the top. Smoke is two layers of puffs, one to each of its 32 by 24 tiles: a wide grey one (150, 150, 164) under a paler one (214 to 230), each nudged off its tile and breathing slowly, thinner as it is wiped; the hand is a rounded outline three tiles wide and five high. Bubbles are pale blue discs (170, 214, 244 at 150) with a white ring and a shine; leaves are green blades and flowers five pink petals round a yellow middle, turning; the boulder is a grey ball on which a crack is drawn for each blow; the flame is three nested flames (red, orange, yellow) that shrink as it is blown; a hole darkens the ground round the player. None of it is pixel art: these are the interface's vector shapes, since they lie over the picture, not in it.
- **The lists** (`ModernUi.DrawUnderground`: the bag below ground, a hiker's trade, a base's PC) are a menu screen of tabs, `ListRow`s with a small picture (a Sphere's ball, a trap's warning sign, a crate for goods, a cut stone for a treasure, a flag) and the value at the right (a Sphere's size, a price as "Red 12"), a detail panel, and SPHERES ON HAND: every Sphere carried as a chip with its ball and size. Paying turns that panel into the choice of which Sphere.
- **A secret base** is the bare cavern of the map, furnished by `UndergroundArt` with the field art kit: the room furniture's own pieces where one fits (`PropModels`: tables, chairs, shelves, a television, a plant) and plain pieces of our own for the rest (a tent, a drum, a cup, a crystal, a plate for a trap's tool). Every doll is one rounded figure in its species' colour, and the big boulder is three round rocks and a pebble: stand-ins, since each would be a sculpt of its own. The PC is the room kit's PC on a desk with a flag on a pole beside it, whose cloth tells the base's rank: red, bronze, silver, gold, platinum.
- **A statue** (plan 38 · EX6, part b; a good of ours, `Data/statues.json`) is a stone figure of its species on a plinth, one tile, painted on the CPU by `UndergroundArt.PaintStatue` (24 by 36 texels, a face of its own per statue, `ug.statue.<good>`): a plinth of dark slate (96, 92, 104) two rows of light on its top edge, with a plaque across its front in the species' first type's colour (`Palette.GetTypeColor`, shaded a third toward violet), and on it the figure in a stone ramp of four greys (light 206, 204, 198; base 166, 162, 158; dark 120, 116, 120; deepest 84, 80, 90), lit from the upper left, outlined in the kit's ink. The figure is the species' Pokédex shape, not its sprite: a four-legged body (quadruped, armor), an upright one (upright, humanoid, legs, arms), a bird with its wings raised (wings, bug wings), a fish arched over the plinth, a coil (squiggle), a round body (ball, blob, heads) or a body with arms of tentacles; one silhouette to a shape, the same for every species of it, told apart by the plaque. The plan asked for the species' own field sprite in a stone palette: field sprites are baked on the graphics card and arrive in later frames, so a face painted from one would differ between runs and could not be built by a test, and the figure is the kit's own. In the wall's game a statue is a shape of two squares by three in the plain treasures' grey (168, 172, 186), its name told once it lies bare.
- **Decorating** (`ModernUi.DrawDecoration`, `DecorationScreen`: the seam Hoenn's secret bases reuse) is a plan of the room seen from straight above on a panel at the left, a square to a tile: the floor in two alternating tones (160, 142, 128) and (152, 134, 122), the tiles that must stay clear lighter (176, 160, 140), every piece a rounded rectangle of its own size in its own colour with the last word of its name on it ("Table", "Doll"; a fixture's own mark, "PC"), as large as the piece has room for. The piece in hand is rimmed green where it may stand and red where it may not; the cursor that picks a piece up is a square's outline in the selection colour. The right-hand panel is the menu (DECORATE, PUT AWAY, MOVE, DONE, under "OUT: n OF limit"), the store's list, or the name of the piece in hand with what to do; the last thing said stands in a panel under it.

### Secret bases

Plan 21 · H10. Hoenn's secret bases, in our own art: nothing of Emerald's decoration tiles, its doll sprites or its base tilesets is taken.

- **The rooms** are Emerald's twenty-four layouts as rooms of the region (`interior` the house's for now: the six kinds' own looks, a cave of red, brown, blue or yellow rock, a hollow tree, a den of grass, are left for a later session). Their PC is the room kit's PC.
- **A decoration** (`DecorationArt`, `PropType.Decoration`) is built by its kind with the field art kit, at the colour its name gives it (its colour word, its pattern's element, its species: red `220,84,70`, blue `84,140,224`, yellow `236,190,64`, green `98,178,96`, pink `238,140,176`, silver `196,204,220`, violet `150,118,200`, brown `190,132,84`, slate `96,104,124`):
  - a **desk** is the rooms' table as long and deep as its shape, a **chair** the rooms' chair, a **plant** the rooms' potted plant on each tile of its front row, a **TV** the rooms' television;
  - a **brick** two bevelled blocks of its colour, the back one 40 rows tall and the front one 22; a **balloon** a round sprite 20 by 40 on its string; a **tent** the Underground's tent; a **board** planks lying flat; a **slide** a grey platform at the back and a run of its colour to the front; a **fence** a low rail along or across its tile; a **tire** a dark ring; a **stand** a low wooden stage; a **mud ball** a brown ball; the **breakable door** a panelled door with a crack across it; the **shields** the Underground's cup and the **glass ornament** its crystal, pale cyan;
  - a **mat** lies flat on the floor, 2 texels in from its tiles' edges: a bordered rug of its colour, a note mat with its note's letter in the middle (a dot under the high C), the big mats of a move each with their move's sign in the deepest shade (a wave, a bolt, a flame, a flake, a heart, a crack, three spikes, a spiral);
  - a **poster** hangs on the face of the wall over the floor, 22 rows up: a pale sheet 26 rows tall in a frame of its colour, with a picture of its own (a ball, waves, a sky with a cloud, a heart, a face) or a plain panel;
  - a **doll** is the Underground's rounded doll in its species' colour on its one tile, a **big doll** the same 30 by 46, two tiles tall on its one; a **cushion** a bevelled pad 5 rows high with its pattern's sign on top.
- **Decorating** is the Underground's screen (`ModernUi.DrawDecoration`): a base that is no rectangle shows its rock dark (`58,50,60`) and the wall a poster may hang on slate (`112,104,116`) on the plan.

### Fossils, the Game Corner, the Villa and Pal Park

Plan 06 · R16b. Everything here is our own: nothing of the original's reel pictures, its mascot's animation, its furniture models or its rooms' art is taken.

- **The museum** (`interior: Museum`) is a hall of pale stone: floor tiles of a warm grey (226, 222, 212) with a darker grid (204, 198, 186), cream walls over a dark wainscot, and its exhibits under glass: a display case (`PropType.DisplayCase`) is a dark plinth with a glass box on it, what lies inside a few lumps of coal black and amber; a wall case is the same against the back wall, taller. The rooms' other furniture is the labs'.
- **The Game Corner's hall** (`interior: GameHall`) is the one loud room of Sinnoh: a carpet of deep red (150, 44, 60) with a small gold lozenge every tile, walls of midnight blue (44, 48, 96) with a band of gold bulbs along their top, a blue counter. A slot machine (`PropType.SlotMachine`) is a cabinet a tile wide and nearly two high: a dark blue body, a lit panel of three reel windows (white, with a red, a gold and a blue mark), a gold tray, a red top light. Machines stand in rows back to back, each turned to the aisle beside it, so the field's camera sees a machine's side and its sloping panel from above.
- **The slot machine** (`SlotMachineScreen`, `ModernUi.DrawSlotMachine`) is one screen in the menus' look on a backdrop of the hall's midnight blue: the cabinet in the middle (a standard panel with a gold frame), its three reels in a window three symbols high whose top and bottom are shaded, a pill under each reel with the key that stops it (lit while that reel turns); the table of what each symbol pays on a panel at the left; the coins, the win being counted and the bet on a panel at the right; over the reels a dark display where the machine lets on what it drew, and where, in the bonus game, Clefairy's menu sprite stands under a moon and an arrow over the reels marks which to stop next. The six symbols are drawn with the kit's shapes, never with letters of the original's: a red seven and a blue seven (the digit in the display face), REPLAY on a green pill, two cherries, a Poké Ball, a yellow crescent moon. The moon over the bonus game is pale yellow, red when winning the game will tire Clefairy, and ringed with light when another round is certain. A line that pays is drawn across the window in gold while its coins are counted.
- **Mauville's slot machines** (plan 21 · H12, part n; `ReelMachineScreen`, `ModernUi.DrawReelMachine`) keep the same cabinet, reels, pays panel and side panel, on a backdrop of night blue (26, 40, 66 to 12, 20, 38). The display over the reels says what to do now in Black 40 on the bolt yellow (252, 214, 64): COINS IN!, STOP THE REELS!, the win, or REEL TIME!. The seven symbols are the kit's shapes and none is a picture of a Pokémon: the two sevens, the cherries and REPLAY as Sinnoh's, Azurill's a blue drop with a pale ball beside it, Lotad's a green round under a lily pad, and Power a lightning bolt of two slanted strokes in the bolt yellow with a dark edge. The lines the coins light are drawn faint in gold across the window (one, three or five); a line that won flashes in full gold while its coins are counted. The side panel shows the coins, three discs for the bet, the payout and Pikachu's gauge as sixteen bolts in two rows, lit as they are won, with A LUCKY GAME! in green under them when it is one. Reel Time's machine is a rounded gold box that slides open from the window's middle over the reels, with a paper face where its six numbers turn (the one in the window in the sevens' red), two bolts bobbing beside it, and a grey cloud where the face was when it blows up.
- **Mauville's roulette** (plan 21 · H12, part o; `RouletteScreen`, `ModernUi.DrawRouletteTable`) is a table of felt, the left table's a deep red (110, 30, 46 to 60, 14, 24) and the right's a green (28, 74, 52 to 14, 40, 30), as Emerald tells its two tables apart. The wheel is a ring of wood with twelve wedges in the board's three colours (orange 236, 136, 48; green 80, 176, 92; purple 150, 92, 200), each with its Pokémon's badge (a round in the Pokémon's own colour with the first letter of its name, none a picture of it), divided by cream spokes and turned with the wheel; a wedge already landed on is darkened, and the wedges of the wager under the cursor blink. The ball is a white round with a glint and a soft shadow, lifted with a shadow under it while it is carried; the balls of the round lie in their slots. The helpers are tokens too: a round of their colour with their name's first letter, Shroomish's shadow growing on the wheel before it drops and the picture shaking with it, Taillow's with two plain wings that beat. The board (three rows of colour by four badges, the colours' heads at the left and the Pokémon's at the top, a square hit darkened with a ball on it) slides away as the ball is thrown and the wheel moves to the middle; the cursor is a gold outline that flashes, round a column, a row or one square, with what it pays under the board. The coins and the balls left are at the top left, the wager at the top right, and the table's line in a panel at the foot, with YES and NO pills when it asks whether to play on.
- **The Villa** is a `House` room and its furniture the houses' own wherever a house has the piece (the table, the sofas, the bed, the television, the bookshelf, the houseplants, the PC, the clock and the painting on the wall); the pieces no house has are built in the same kit and woods: a piano (a dark lacquered case with a white keyboard), a rack and a night table (shelves of the bookshelf's wood, a lamp on the night table), a writing desk with a computer on it, a bed and a television as wide as their tiles, an audio system (a dark cabinet with two speaker cones), a bust on a plinth (bronze, or silver), a guest set (a low table with a seat at each end), and the small things that stand on others (a music box on the rack, a tea set on the guest table) as sprites a few texels high. The chandelier hangs out of the field camera's sight and is not drawn.
- **Pal Park's lobby** is a `PokemonCenter`-like public room in the gate houses' look (`interior: Lab`), its counter across the middle; the park itself is country of the imported world, drawn as any route is.
- **The Catching Show's count** (`ModernUi.ParkCount`) is the Safari Game's panel at the top right with the Park Balls left and the seconds run.
- **The Coin Case's count** (`ModernUi.CoinCount`) is the same panel at the top left, a little wider, with the coins and the money side by side; it stands there while someone of the Game Corner or its prize exchange is spoken to, as the original's two windows do.
- **The card-flip game** (plan 42 · PS3; `CardFlipScreen`, `ModernUi.DrawCardFlip`) is one screen in the menus' look on the hall's midnight blue (`HallBlue` to `HallDeep`) with its bulbs along the top. Everything on it is ours: nothing of HeartGold's card art, its board, its mascot or its words. At the left a table of deep teal felt (24, 92, 96 to 12, 54, 60) holds five rows of five cards, each 132 units square with 14 between; at each row's right end and under each column's foot a clue tile of the same size in one of five colours (row or column 1 to 5: coral 232, 120, 96; green 96, 176, 104; gold 236, 180, 64; blue 80, 140, 226; violet 156, 104, 214) shows the line's sum in Black 40 over a small cracked tile and the line's busts beside it in Black 32. A card face down is cream (246, 240, 224) with a gold lattice of three diamonds and a gold rim; turned, it is paper white with its number in the display face, the number's own band of colour along its top (1 slate 120, 132, 156; 2 green; 3 gold); a bust is a slate tile (70, 76, 92) split by a jagged crack of light from corner to corner with two chips beside it, never a picture of a Pokémon. A card turns over in a quarter of a second, squeezed to nothing across its width and opened again. The memo's four marks sit in a card's four corners as small pips: a crack at the top left for a bust, 1 at the top right, 2 at the bottom left, 3 at the bottom right, each in its colour. The cursor is a gold outline 6 units thick round the card. At the right a standard panel shows LEVEL with its number large, the coins in the case, the round's coins in gold, the four memo buttons as pills in their colours (lit while that mark is on the card under the cursor) and a red QUIT pill. The round's line (what a card gave, a bust, a round cleared) stands in a strip under the panel. Before a round a prompt asks PLAY LEVEL n? with PLAY and LEAVE; the prompt's words say whether the level rose or fell since the last round. Quitting asks the menus' prompt with the coins it would keep; after a round every card turns face up for a look before the board is cleared.

### The Battle Frontier

Plan 06 · R18a. Everything here is our own: nothing of the original's rooms, record screens or trainers' pictures is taken.

- **The Battle Tower's rooms** are the museum's hall of pale stone (`interior: Museum`): the lobby thirty-one tiles wide, three booths along its back each a gap in a blue counter with an attendant in it, a machine on the counter beside each booth, a lift's doorway in the back wall behind the west and the middle booth, an alcove with a statue at either end and potted plants on the floor. The lift is a room five tiles square with a mat at its door; the corridors are one row of floor before a back wall of doorways four tiles apart; a battle room is bare but for a plant in each corner, the opponent's doorway in the middle of its back wall (two side by side in the room for two). A doorway is the rooms' own (dark, framed, a yellow lintel).
- **The Battle Frontier's board** (`FrontierRecordsScreen`, `ModernUi.DrawFrontierBoard`) is one screen in the menus' look, titled BATTLE RECORDS: a standard panel with the facility's name at its head in Black 48 and, at the head's right, the print as a disc 38 in radius with a ring (the rule colour and NO PRINT YET until one is won, silver (168, 178, 198), gold (232, 180, 60)); under three small captions a card for each way the challenge is taken, its name at 40, the record streak in Black 56 and the latest in Black 44, muted once the streak has ended and followed by a green RUNNING tag while it runs; and a foot strip in the rule colour with the Battle Points and the battles won there.
- **Pennant Hall** (`Hub_Plaza`, plan 41 · EG3) is the museum's hall of pale stone (`interior: Museum`), 23 tiles by 15: eight pitches of three tiles by three, four along the north wall and four in a row across the hall's middle, each empty floor until a stall stands there, and then a **booth** of the Battle Frontier's (`PropType.Booth`) whose awning is striped in the stall's own colour (`Prop.Tint`, `Landmarks.AwningTone`: the goods' red `214,82,66`, the lottery's blue `58,108,214`, the Seals' gold `226,184,78`, the Poffins' pink `238,124,156`, the relearner's green `92,170,96`, Kurt's purple `150,98,170`, the blender's teal `42,138,138`) and white, its keeper standing in the booth's middle and spoken to across its counter. A blackboard three tiles wide on the back wall between the second and third pitches is the Week's Work; a plant in each corner and a rug inside the door. The day's guests stand about the aisles in their classes' looks; the manager, Quincy Rowan-Pike, stands by the door in a dark fedora with a red band and a claret coat.
- **The Battle Points' count** (`ModernUi.PointsCount`) is the Coin Case's panel at the top left, with the points in Black 36; it stands there while a booth of the Exchange Service Corner or a battle room speaks of them.
- **Entering a team** is the team's own screen: a Pokémon chosen carries a green tag with its place (1ST, 2ND, 3RD, 4TH) where a field move's tag would be, one that may not go in a grey NOT ABLE, and the prompt at the foot counts how many are still to go and then asks whether to go in.
- **A Frontier battle** is fought on a Gym's stage; its two questions (the Bag's refusal, RUN's "give the match up?") are the battle's message box and its Yes and No.
- **The Battle Factory's rooms** (plan 06 · R18b). The lobby is the museum's hall on a level floor: the way in at the middle of its west wall on three tiles of mat, six benches in pairs either side of the aisle from the door, a screen on its stand against the back wall, and in the east an L of blue counter with an attendant in each of its two gaps, the records' machine between them and a doorway in the back wall behind. The corridor and the battle room are a laboratory's rooms (`interior: Lab`), fifteen tiles by nine: tall machines along the corridor's back wall either side of the door and a terminal against each side wall, the staff standing before the door; the battle room bare but for a long rug across its middle, a machine in each back corner, a terminal either side of the door and a plant in each front corner, the challenger at the rug's west end and the trainer at its east.
- **The Battle Factory's screen** (`FactoryScreen`, `ModernUi.DrawFactory`) is one screen in the menus' look, titled BATTLE FACTORY. Renting: the six on offer as cards in a row under the caption ON OFFER, each a pale disc with the Pokémon's menu sprite at twice its size, its name and gender in Black 32 and its level in muted Black 22 (no more is shown until its summary is asked for, as in the original); the card under the cursor is raised ten units with the selection's border, a card already chosen has a green border and a green disc 30 in radius at its top left with its place (1, 2, 3). Under them a panel captioned with what the three places are, each a slot with its numbered disc (the rule colour until filled), the Pokémon's name and its type pills. Along the foot a panel 140 tall with the question of the moment in ExtraBold 40 and, while a Pokémon's menu is up, its options as pill buttons at the right: SUMMARY blue, RENT and EXCHANGE green, REMOVE the selection's colour, CANCEL muted. A trade: one's own three under YOUR RENTALS and the beaten trainer's under their name, cards 300 by 330, the one being given up tagged GIVING UP, the trainer's three dimmed until they are chosen from; CANCEL beside the first row, BACK and CANCEL beside the second, and at the right a panel THE TRADE that names what is given up and what is taken, an arrow between. A yes or a no is the menus' prompt over the dimmed screen, and a trade made is said by the same prompt with one button.
- **A rental's summary** is the team's summary of one page (stats and moves) titled RENTAL POKÉMON, with the ability beside the nature and the held item under them where a Pokémon of one's own shows its experience.
- **The Factory's rows on the board** are four (Level 50 and the Open Level, each Single and Double), 112 tall where the Tower's three are 132, each with its trades in muted Black 22 after its record streak and in Black 20 after its latest.

#### The Battle Hall and the Battle Castle

Plan 06 · R18c.

- **The Battle Hall's rooms** are a game hall's (`interior: GameHall`: the red carpet with its gold lozenges, navy walls with a gold rail). The lobby is twenty-seven tiles by fourteen on a level floor: the way in at the middle of its east wall on a mat, a long counter in the west with the two attendants in the gap under it, the records' machine at the counter's end with the record keeper beside it, a screen and a PC against the back wall between plants, two benches, six cushions before the counter, and the way down to the stage in the front wall behind the attendants. The passage is nine tiles wide with a runner down its middle and a plant in each corner. The stage is a room seventeen tiles by fifteen: a rug seven tiles long across its middle is the stage itself, the challenger at its west end and the trainer at its east, a runner leading to it from the door in the back wall, the announcer west of the rug, and the crowd standing along the side walls and the front (four, eight from a streak's third round, twelve from its sixth).
- **The Battle Hall's board** (`HallBoardScreen`, `ModernUi.DrawHallBoard`) is one screen in the menus' look, titled BATTLE HALL: twenty cells in five rows of four. A type's card has the type's colour as a strip down its left edge, its name in Black 36, RANK in muted Black 20 and the rank it stands at in Black 54 at its right, and ten pips 16 across along its foot, one filled in the type's colour for each win over it; the card under the cursor has the selection's border, the type agreed to a green one, and a type beaten ten times is washed out under a muted CLEARED tag clear of its last pip. The entered Pokémon's card spans two cells of the last row: its menu sprite on a pale disc (both, for a Double challenge), its name and gender in 40, its level and type pills, and a blue SUMMARY tag. The last cell is "? ? ?" and NOBODY YET, washed out, until the Hall Matron is announced: then it is lit with a gold border and says HALL MATRON, and every type is washed out. A foot panel 116 tall says what is asked in ExtraBold 38, with BATTLE (n / 10) and STREAK at its right in Black 44. A type is agreed to in the menus' prompt, titled with the type and its rank; the Hall Matron's notice is the same prompt with one button.
- **The Hall's rows on the board of records** are two, and under them, captioned BY POKÉMON, up to eight cells in two rows of four in the rule colour, each a species' name in ExtraBold 28 with SINGLE and DOUBLE in muted Black 18 and its two records in Black 26 at the right (muted where nought).
- **The Battle Castle's rooms.** The lobby is a hall of the League's stone (`interior: Gym`, `hall: League`), twenty-three tiles by seventeen on a level floor: the way in at the middle of its east wall on a mat, the way into the castle in the west between two blocks of wall, the two attendants across it with the records' machine and the PC beside them, a screen against the back wall, white parapets with blue fronts making two bays with a table and cushions in each, and two statues on plinths in the south. The valet's room is the same stone, nineteen tiles by twelve: four statues along its back wall, a runner along its length, a second runner up to the door in the back wall, the valet standing where they meet. The throne room is the Champion's hall (`hall: Champion`), seventeen tiles square: the lady at the head of the room between two plants, a statue in each back corner, a carpet at the room's middle with a runner through it from the door to the head of the room, the challenger west of the carpet's middle and the trainer east.
- **The Battle Castle's counters** (`CastleScreen`, `ModernUi.DrawCastle`) are one screen in the menus' look, titled BATTLE CASTLE · YOUR POKÉMON or BATTLE CASTLE · THE NEXT TRAINER. Three cards 368 by 520 in a row: a Pokémon's menu sprite at twice its size on a pale disc 118 in radius, its name and gender in 36 and its level in muted Black 28; one's own with its HP bar and, under three small captions, its HP, the PP its moves have left between them and what it holds; one of the trainer's a ring and a question mark and "? ? ?" until it is identified, with a tag for each thing bought about it (FIVE LEVELS UP in the selection's colour, FIVE LEVELS DOWN blue, STATS TOLD and MOVES TOLD green). Under the cards a panel with the Castle Points in Black 64, the counter's ranks as discs 22 in radius (gold with a white digit when held, a ring in the rule colour otherwise) and EXIT as a muted pill. At the right a panel the height of both: what the counter is for, in muted ExtraBold 28; or the menu that is up, its head in Black 40 and its options as pills 92 tall with the price in Castle Points at the right, or the rank that is wanted (blue; muted where the rank doesn't reach; RANK UP gold, HIGHEST once it is; CANCEL muted); or what is lent, seven list rows with their prices, a track and thumb at the right when there are more, and the description of the one under the cursor at the foot; or a Pokémon's moves as cards with their type pills and PP; or one of the trainer's stats, six rows in the rule colour and its nature, ability and item under captions (never its moves, which are bought apart). A price is agreed to, and what came of it said, in the menus' prompt. One's own Pokémon's summary is the rental's page, titled YOUR POKÉMON.
- **The Castle Points' count** (`ModernUi.CastlePointsCount`) is the Battle Points' panel at the top left with the points in Black 36, and stands there while the valet speaks of them.
- **The Castle's rows on the board of records** are two, each with the Castle Points of its streak after it, as the Factory's rows have their trades.

#### The Battle Arcade

Plan 06 · R18d.

- **The Battle Arcade's rooms** are a game hall's, as the Battle Hall's are (`interior: GameHall`). The lobby is twenty-three tiles by seventeen on a level floor: the way in at the middle of its west wall on three tiles of mat; in the back wall the doorway to the corridor, with a counter running out from the wall at its west side, the two attendants standing under it, and the records' machine and the PC east of them before a second counter; a screen on the back wall; and across the room's south two inner walls with a gap at alternate ends, where the original's floor steps down to its lounge, and the lounge's plants and people beyond them. The corridor is sixteen tiles by thirteen: a runner from its door to the doorway in its back wall, two screens on that wall and a plant in each corner. The game room is seventeen tiles by eighteen: a great screen on the back wall (four `Television` props side by side) with the host before it, a rug across the room's upper middle with the challenger at its west end and the trainer at its east, a runner from the door to the rug, and the crowd standing in two blocks either side of the runner.
- **The game board** (`ArcadeBoardScreen`, `ModernUi.DrawArcadeBoard`) is one screen in the menus' look, titled BATTLE ARCADE: sixteen panels in four rows of four at the screen's middle, each 200 by 154 with 16 between, the challenger's three in a column of cards at its left and the trainer's three at its right. **A panel's colour says whom its event falls on**, and nothing else does: red for the trainer to come, blue for the challenger, green for both (the battle and the board), gold for a bonus; a row of four tags over the board (FOE, YOU, BOTH, BONUS) is the key. A panel is a card with a band of its colour 96 tall holding the event's sign in white, drawn from the kit's shapes (our own signs: a bar and an arrow for HP down, a drop, a bolt, a flame, Zzz, a flake, a berry, a box, an arrow and "+3", a sun, a cloud with drops or with stones, drifts of sand, bars of fog, a turned square, chevrons, a die, two arrows, a coin with its number, a crossed ring, a dash), and the event's name under it in Black 24. While the panels count down each is a plain card with the digit in Black 96 in the rule colour. The cursor is the selection's border 8 wide round a panel, with the selection's glow behind; once it is stopped every panel shows the event it stopped on, as the original's do, and the foot names it. A side's card is 420 by 154 (what the board leaves of the screen, less the margin): the Pokémon's menu sprite on a pale disc, its name in ExtraBold 28, its level in muted Black 22, and what it holds as a small tag (muted NOTHING HELD otherwise); the side's heading (YOU, or the trainer's name) is in white Black 28 over the column. The foot panel is 116 tall: what is to be done in ExtraBold 38 (the count, "Press Z to stop the cursor!", the event's name), and at its right under captions the cursor's speed as eight pips 16 across (filled in the selection's colour up to the speed; RANDOM in a gold tag 26 tall beside the caption when it jumps), BATTLE (n / 7) and STREAK in Black 44. A bar 8 tall along the foot's top edge runs down with the thirty seconds the cursor may run.
- **The Arcade's rows on the board of records** are two, a streak each and nothing beside it.

#### The bracket

Plan 14 · B8 (`BracketScreen`, `ModernUi.DrawBracket`); plan 14 · B11's World Tournament seats its eight on the same.

- **The Battle Frontier's annex** (`BattleFrontierAnnex`, `BattleDome`) are a game hall's rooms (`interior: GameHall`), ours. The lobby is fifteen tiles by eleven: the way out at the middle of its front wall, a counter seven tiles long against the back wall with the Dome's receptionist before it, a plant in each corner and a runner from the door to the desk. The Dome's hall is seventeen tiles by fifteen, as the Battle Hall's stage is: a rug seven tiles square at its middle is the ring, the challenger at its south edge and the trainer at its north, the announcer west of it and the crowd along the side walls and the front.
- **The tree** is one screen in the menus' look, titled BATTLE DOME, with the round under way (FIRST ROUND, QUARTERFINALS, SEMIFINALS, FINAL) in white Black 34 centred over it. Eight seats down each side, each a card 320 by 76 on a row of 92 from 56 under the content's top: the trainer's name in Black 30 and the class in muted Black 18 under it (the player's name and YOU). The player's seat has a blue border, the next opponent's a gold one with a gold NEXT tag at its right, and the one under the cursor the selection's border; an entrant who has lost is washed out. From each seat a line runs in, then turns up or down to where its match meets, four steps across each half's 566 units between the seats and the middle; a line is 4 wide in white at a third, and the side that won is drawn 7 wide in gold, or in blue for the player. The final's place is a panel 260 by 92 at the middle with FINAL in Black 30, and once the tournament is over the champion's name with CHAMPION in gold Black 20 under it in a gold frame. The foot panel 96 tall says what is asked in ExtraBold 34, with STREAK (the tournaments won in a row before this one) in Black 40 at its right.
- **A card** is a panel 920 by 620 over the tree dimmed: the trainer's class and name in Black 42, how far they have come in the label's style (the round they stand in, OUT IN THE round, or CHAMPION), and their three, each a card 116 tall: the menu icon on its disc, the name and gender in 34, the level at 50 in muted Black 24 and the type pills.
- **The pick** is two panels side by side over the tree dimmed: at the left the opponent's card (THEIR THREE), at the right the player's three (YOUR THREE, TWO GO INTO BATTLE) as the same cards, a chosen one with a green border and a green SENT IN tag; under them BATTLE, a red pill 300 by 80, muted until two are chosen, with a white border under the cursor.

- **The Masters' Hall** (`MastersHall`, plan 14 · B15) is a game hall's room of ours, eleven tiles by thirteen, come to by a door in the middle of the annex lobby's west wall (between the Palace's and the Pike's receptionists) and left by the middle of its own front wall. A runner three tiles wide runs from the master's place, three tiles in from the back wall, to the mark the challenger is put on five tiles south of it; the receptionist stands west of the runner by the door, a plant in each corner. Nobody stands there before the Hall of Fame: the room is empty and quiet until then. The master is one person whatever species is put forward, drawn as the black belt's class until plan 11 gives masters a look of their own; he steps down the runner to the mark's north tile for the battle and back to his place after it.

#### The World Tournament

Plan 14 · B11 (`TournamentScreen`, `ModernUi.DrawTournament`): the bracket's look, at eight.

- **The hall** (`WorldTournamentHall`) is the Dome's hall laid out again, ours: a game hall's room seventeen tiles by fifteen, the stage a rug seven tiles square, the challenger at its south edge and the opponent walking down onto its north, the announcer west of it and the crowd along the walls. The desk is the annex lobby's, its receptionist at the east wall's foot beside the Pyramid's.
- **The events** are one screen in the menus' look, titled WORLD TOURNAMENT: a card for each event 196 tall on a pitch of 224, as wide as the content less 400, at its middle, with the event's name in Black 40, what it is in muted ExtraBold 26 and the titles won in Black 24; the Battle Points a title earns on a gold tag at its top right. The cursor is the selection's border with a gold one. **A locked event** is washed out, its name muted, with a muted LOCKED tag and the event whose title opens it in its last line; A on it is the error sound. The foot panel 96 tall says what is asked in ExtraBold 34.
- **The tree** is the bracket's with four seats down each side, each a card 380 by 96 on a row of 168 from 90 under the content's top (the name in Black 34, the class in muted Black 20), three steps across each half to the final's panel (280 by 100) in the middle; the event's name in muted Black 26 over the round's (QUARTERFINALS, SEMIFINALS, FINAL) in white Black 34. Lines, borders, the NEXT tag, an entrant out washed and the champion's frame are the bracket's.
- **A card** is the bracket's card: the trainer's class and name, how far they have come, and their three at level 50.
- **More events than fit** (plan 14 · B12: every region's Leaders and League, the world's events, the formats and the type brackets): three cards are shown at once (`TournamentScreen.VisibleRows`), the window following the cursor as every list's does, with a thin track 10 wide 28 to the right of the cards and a thumb in the frame's colour, as the lists' scroll bar.

#### The gift box

Plan 40 · GE7 (`GiftBoxScreen`, `ModernUi.DrawGiftBox`). Our own throughout. One screen in the menus' look, titled GIFT BOX, opened by a Gift Runner's `gift open`; its colour is the Gift Runners' scarf, a warm yellow (240, 196, 70), which is a tab's accent, a card's ribbon band and the selection's second border.

- **Three tabs** along the content's top as every menu's tabs: WAITING (the gifts due), CARDS (the album of gifts taken) and TELL WORDS. The tab's count of waiting gifts is a yellow tag beside its word.
- **A card** is a panel 420 by 300, three to a row on a pitch of 460, the content's width at its middle: a yellow band 48 tall across its top with the sender's name in Black 26, the picture in a well 200 by 150 (a Pokémon's or an Egg's menu sprite at twice its size, an item's icon at three times, a Seal or a ribbon as the capsule and ribbon pages draw them, a backdrop as its swatch), the gift's title in Black 30 and its card's words in muted ExtraBold 22, wrapped to two lines. On CARDS the day it was taken stands in the band's right in Black 22.
- **The cursor** is the selection's border with a yellow one inside it. With nothing waiting WAITING says so in the middle, in muted ExtraBold 30.
- **TELL WORDS** is one card of the same size in the middle, its picture a speech bubble in yellow, saying that two words told to a Gift Runner may call a gift up; Confirm on it closes the box and the runner asks for the words on the phrase picker.
- A **Gift Runner** is a courier of ours (the user's 18b): the look `gift_runner` of `characters.json`, a navy jacket, a cap and a scarf both in the box's yellow (240, 196, 70); no new rig, the people's kit.

#### The trade board

Plan 40 · GE8 (`TradeBoardScreen`, `ModernUi.DrawTradeBoard`). Our own. One screen in the menus' look, titled TRADE BOARD with the region's name in a muted tag after it; its colour is a link-desk teal (60, 170, 180).

- **The day's three requests** are cards 560 by 380 side by side on a pitch of 600, at the content's middle: a teal band 52 tall with the trainer's name in Black 28 and, for a trainer from abroad, their language on a white tag; below it two wells 220 by 180 side by side with an arrow between, WANTS (the species' menu sprite at twice its size, its name in Black 26 and "Lv. N or higher" in muted ExtraBold 22; a species not yet seen is the menu sprite's silhouette in the frame's colour and its name "???") and OFFERS (the same, with the held item's icon at its corner). A request met is greyed under a teal MET stamp.
- **The blind trade** is a row 1720 wide under the cards, 120 tall: a teal "?" in a circle, BLIND TRADE in Black 32, and how many are left today (of five) in Black 28 at its right.
