### The Pokéathlon

Plan 15 · E6 (`AthlonScreen`, `ModernUi.DrawAthlon`). Our own throughout: nothing of HeartGold's dome, events or
screens is drawn. One screen in the menus' look, titled POKÉATHLON (the course's name over an event). **A team's colour
says whose a thing is**, and nothing else does: the player's in the selection's colour, the rivals blue, green and gold
in the order drawn; a team is named on a chip of its colour (the player's name, or TEAM and a name of ours). The dome's
own colour is a sky blue (58, 150, 214): an event card's band, a stat's stars and the clock's bar.

- **The course choice**: a list panel 860 wide at the content's left, captioned COURSES, a row for each course with its
  best score at the right (NOT RUN YET until it has one); at the right a panel captioned EVENTS with a card 150 tall for
  each of the course's three on a pale wash of the dome's blue, its number in Black 64 in that blue, its name in Black 40
  and the stat it leans on in muted ExtraBold 26; along the foot the Athlete Points.
- **The team**: the team's rows (an Egg muted, with EGG at its right), each chosen one with a green tag 64 by 44 holding
  its place (1, 2, 3); at the right the one under the cursor on a pale disc at twice its icon's size, its name in Black
  44, PERFORMANCE in muted Black 24, and its five stats as rows: the stat's name in ExtraBold 32 and five discs 30 across,
  filled in the dome's blue up to the stat and in the rule colour after it. The foot counts the three chosen.
- **An event's briefing**: a card 1240 by 780 at the screen's middle, its top 130 a band of the dome's blue with EVENT
  n OF 3 in white Black 26, the event's name in white Black 60 and what it leans on at the band's right; under it how it
  is played in Bold 34, wrapped to the card; and the player's three in a row, each on a pale disc 80 in radius with its
  name and its stars in the stat the event leans on.
- **An event** fills the screen under a bar 92 tall with the event's name in Black 40 and the clock in Black 44 at its
  right (Red in the last five seconds), a line of the dome's blue along its foot running down with the time. The count
  before it is a dark disc 190 in radius at the middle with the digit (then GO!) in white Black, shrinking within each
  second. Each Pokémon is its menu icon at twice its size.
  - **Hurdle Dash**: four lanes 176 tall, alternate brick reds (206, 112, 82) and (196, 102, 74), a white line along each
  floor; hurdles white posts with a gold bar, hand-over lines faint white and the finish solid white; the view follows
  the player's runner, a unit of the track 1.2 of the layout; a runner rises in a parabola 90 high through its jump and
  is half faded while it gets up from a trip; one out of the view is an arrow of its team's colour at the lane's edge.
  Each lane's chip at its top left and its leg (or the time it finished in) beside it.
  - **Block Smash**: four columns, each a panel (the player's framed in its colour) with the team's chip, the blocks
  broken in Black 72, a stack of five tan blocks 180 by 56 whose top one cracks in dark strokes as it is hit and shakes
  four units for the frames after a hit, the hitter under it (faded while it is changed), and the three's breath as bars
  in the rule colour (the hitter's in the team's colour, a tired one's Red).
  - **Ring Drop**: the platform at the stage's middle, a unit 3.6 of the layout: a sand disc on a dark rim with a blue
  ring near its edge and a paler one at its middle; each Pokémon on a disc of its team's colour with a white face, a
  glow behind it while it lunges; each team's corner panel (the player's at the bottom right) with its chip, its
  knock-offs and falls, and, while it is off, the next of its three faded.
  - **Pennant Capture** (plan 15 · E7): a lawn (96, 168, 84) on a dark rim at the stage's middle, a unit 5.6 of the
  layout, a white line across its middle and a white ring at its centre; each pennant a white pole with a gold flag;
  each Pokémon on a disc of its team's colour with a white face (faded while it is changed, three gold sparks circling
  it while it is dazed); each team's panel 200 wide at its own corner, outside the lawn, with its chip, its pennants in
  Black 56 and its runner's breath as a bar (the team's colour, Red once tired).
  - **Snow Throw** (plan 15 · E7): a square of snow (226, 236, 246) on a dark rim at the stage's middle, a unit 5 of the
  layout, each team on a disc of its colour at its side (the player's at the bottom), a triangle of its colour pointing
  at whom it throws at, and a bar under it filling as its next throw is made up; one ducking is drawn smaller and lower,
  one dazed faded with gold sparks; a snowball a white disc with a pale blue rim, rising in an arc 80 high on its way,
  its shadow on the snow under it. The corner panels are Ring Drop's, with its hits and the balls it ducked.
  - **Disc Catch** (plan 15 · E7): four lanes as Block Smash's columns, each a pale wash of the dome's blue with a lawn
  strip 70 tall along its foot; the team's chip, its catches in Black 56; a disc a gold ellipse 44 by 16 with a white
  rim, its shadow a dark ellipse on the lawn under it; the catcher rising with its jump, a glow of its team's colour
  for the frames after a catch.
  - **Lamp Jump** (plan 15 · E7): four columns of night (30, 40, 70), each with its chip, a springboard of its team's
  colour at the foot and a column of ten lamps up its right side, a unit 1.8 of the layout to each unit of height: a
  lamp the last bounce's top passed is gold with a glow, the rest dark grey; the jumper over the springboard at its
  height; the lamps lit in all in Black 32 under the board.
- **An event's points**: a row 152 tall for each team (the player's on a pale wash of its colour), its chip, its three,
  and a bar of its colour growing over 0.8 s to its points, the number in Black 56 at the right.
- **The scoreboard**: SCOREBOARD; the four in order, each a row 144 tall with its place in Black 64 (gold for the
  first), its chip and three, its points in each event under the events' names and its total in Black 56; along the
  foot the points earned and held, and a gold NEW RECORD! pill when the course's best was beaten.
- **Events, batch 3** (plan 15 · E8):
  - **Relay Run**: an oval track 1500 by 600 at the stage's middle in the lanes' brick red with four white lane lines;
  each team's runner on its own lane, a disc 30 in radius of its team's colour with a white face and its icon on it, a
  spurt a glow of its colour behind it; at the track's middle each team's laps in Black 56 beside its chip, and its
  three's breath as bars as in Block Smash.
  - **Circle Push**: the floor a square 760 across in pale stone (226, 220, 204) with a dark rim; the circle at its middle
  a gold ring 6 thick round a pale gold wash, brighter while one Pokémon has it alone; the Pokémon as in Ring Drop; each
  team's corner panel with its chip and its points.
  - **Goal Roll**: a pitch 760 across in two greens (98, 168, 92) and (88, 156, 84) in bands, white lines, each side's
  goal a mouth of its team's colour; the ball a white disc 22 in radius with a dark rim; the Pokémon as in Ring Drop;
  each team's corner panel with its chip and its goals.
- **Medals** (plan 15 · E8, by score bands of our own, `Data/athlon.json`): gold (232, 184, 48), silver (184, 192, 204)
  and bronze (196, 128, 72) discs with a darker rim and a white star; on the scoreboard's foot the course's medal at the
  left of NEW RECORD!, nothing under bronze.
- **The records board** (`athlon records`): RECORDS; a row 96 tall for each course with its name in Black 40, its best
  score in Black 48 at the right and its best medal's disc before it (an empty rule-coloured ring where none is won);
  along the foot the courses run and won and the Athlete Points.
- **Bonuses** (plan 15, its last part): on an event's points, under each row's bar, a pill 40 tall for each bonus the
  team won, the dome's sky blue for a Steady one and gold (232, 184, 48) for a Star one, its name and +2 in white Black
  22, 12 apart; the bar and its number are the event's points alone. On the scoreboard the events' columns are 200 wide
  from 760 into the board, and a fourth, BONUS, holds each team's bonus points in Black 40 in the muted ink (a dash where
  it won none), before TOTAL, which counts them.
- **The Athlete Shop** is the shop's screen with the dome's stock: the prices and the purse in Athlete Points, a number
  with PTS after it in place of the money's sign, the purse captioned ATHLETE POINTS; it opens on its list (there is
  nothing to sell).

### Training

Plan 36 · TR2 (`TrainingScreen`, `ModernUi.DrawTraining`). Our own throughout: nothing of X and Y's Super Training (its
balloons, goals or bags) is drawn. One screen in the menus' look, titled TRAINING. The club's own colour is a warm orange
(232, 112, 64): the briefing's band, the clock's bar, the target's middle, the effort a drill added; a shot or a pellet of
energy (the special drills') is violet (150, 110, 236). A grade's medal is the Pokéathlon's (gold, silver, bronze discs
with a darker rim and a white star). The trainee is its own menu sprite (128 texels), never its field sprite: the drills
are drawn in layout units where a field sprite would want the field's renderer.

- **The stat chosen**: at the left a panel 560 wide with the trainee on a pale disc at twice its sprite's size, its name
  in Black 48, its level, EFFORT with its total out of 510 and a bar, and a line that the drills are free; at the right a
  panel with a row 112 tall for each of the six stats (in the drills' order: Attack, Sp. Atk, Defense, Sp. Def, Speed,
  HP), the stat's name in Black 36 and its drill's in muted ExtraBold 24 under it, an effort bar 420 by 14 (Selection,
  Gold at the cap) with `n / 252`, and the best score at the right (NOT TRAINED YET before any) with a small gold medal
  before it once the stat has earned a gold; the row under the cursor a pale wash of Selection with its rim. A stat the
  drills refuse is muted, and choosing it says why in Red in the foot panel, which otherwise says what to do.
- **A drill's briefing**: a card 1240 by 720 at the middle, its top 130 a band of the club's orange with "Trains {stat}"
  in white Black 26, the drill's name in white Black 60 and its length at the band's right; how it is played in Bold 34,
  wrapped; and the three medals in a row, radius 70, each with its name, the score it wants and the effort it gives in
  the club's orange.
- **A drill** fills the screen under a bar 92 tall with the drill's name, its score (after a small medal of the grade it
  stands at) and the clock (Red in the last five seconds), the club's orange running down along its foot. The count before
  it is the Pokéathlon's dark disc with the digit and GO!.
  - **The throw**: the back wall a panel of pale stone (226, 214, 192) 520 tall with faint upright seams, the target on it
    a cream board, a blue ring and an orange middle, shrinking with the drill; under it the floor a blue panel with a white
    line, the aim a white line with a chevron at its head and the trainee over it at one and a half times its sprite; a shot
    a tan disc (a violet glow for the energy shot) flying from the trainee to the wall and shrinking as it goes; where the
    last one struck a dark mark and its points (MISS, muted) rising for two thirds of a second.
  - **The guard**: three lanes, each a panel of pale stone, the trainee's lit a pale wash of the club's orange, with the
    guard's line across each; a pellet a dark ball with a glint (a violet one with a glow in the shield drill) falling to
    the line; the trainee at the foot of its lane, a shield (blue, violet in the shield drill) raised over it on the line
    while the guard is up, faded and circled by three gold sparks while stunned; a pellet settled says its points, or MISS
    in Red, over its lane.
  - **The dash**: a sky panel (196, 222, 244) over a track in the lanes' brick red with a white line and marks every
    hundred units going by; hurdles white posts with a gold bar; the trainee a third of the way along, rising through a jump
    in an arc 170 high and faded while it gets up from a stumble; in the sky the pace as a bar 640 long in the club's orange,
    the long run's stamina under it (Green, Red under a quarter), the streak and the hurdles cleared in Black 32 and the
    distance run, and at the right the beat: an orange disc with a ring closing on it at each beat.
- **The grade**: a card 1040 wide with the drill's name, the medal at radius 140 over a soft glow of its colour, the grade in
  Black 72, the score, the effort given ("+24 effort in Attack!", the club's orange; muted when none fits) and a gold NEW
  BEST! pill when the score is the stat's best.
- **The effort after**: one panel with the trainee and, for each of the six, its name (the stat trained in the club's
  orange), a bar 520 by 16 with the effort before in Selection and what the drill added in the club's orange, `n / 252`
  and, where it changed, a drawn arrow and the new number, and at the right the stat's value ("71 to 74" where it rose);
  the total out of 510 at the foot.

### The Stroll Counter

Plan 35 · BN7 (`StrollCounterScreen`, `ModernUi.DrawStroll`). A device of ours, nothing of the Pokéwalker's shape or
screen: a pebble-shaped shell (1040 wide, corners of 120) in persimmon 232 120 72 with a darker rim 176 78 46, and in it a
screen in **two tones**, pale green 196 214 158 and ink 44 66 40, with a middle green 126 152 96 for rules and what is used
up; a 4-wide ink rim round the screen, corners of 26. Everything on the screen is drawn in those tones: the course's name
(30 Black) at the top left and STRIDES and SPARKS at the top right (28 ExtraBold) over a rule; the Pokémon riding in it as
its menu sprite at 2× **tinted to the ink** (a silhouette, as an old screen shows it). **Home**: the rider at the left, the
menu (SEEK, MEET, COURSE, CLOSE, 34 Black) at the right, the row chosen as pale letters on an ink bar, and how many met
and found it holds under the menu. **Seek**: three bushes of three ink discs side by side, one looked in turned to the
middle green, the find a pale dot in it, an ink arrow over the bush under the cursor. **Meet**: the rider at the left,
the Pokémon met at the right (both inked), two hearts under each (filled while kept, rings once lost), and STRIKE, DODGE,
CATCH in a row under them. **Courses**: the open courses as rows. The line of what happened is white under the screen,
a refusal in the rim's darker persimmon.

### The Power Watch

Plan 36 · TR8 (`PowersScreen`, `ModernUi.DrawPowers`, `ModernUi.PowerChips`). Our own: powers are named plainly by what
they do, with icons of ours, and nothing of the O-Powers' or Roto Powers' pictures is drawn. A kind's **icon** is a disc
in its own colour (EXP blue 70 136 232, Effort the club's orange 232 112 64, Prize gold 214 160 40, Bargain green 56 168
120, Catching red 226 72 84, Encounter grass 96 176 64, Stealth violet 110 100 168, Hatching pink 230 130 170, Type teal
60 160 176, Gathering brown 150 120 90) with a 3-wide rim darkened by a fifth and its short mark (`powerWatch.short.*`, EXP,
EV, $, %, CAT, ENC, SNK, EGG...) in white Black, 0.74 of the radius (0.62 for a mark of four letters).

- **The panel**: one screen in the menus' look, titled POWER WATCH. At the left a list 1060 wide of rows 24 from the
  top: today's free power first where there is one ("Today: EXP Power", its value "Lv. 1 FREE"), then each kind learned
  (`ListRow`, the icon at radius 30, 70 in; its value "Lv. 2 · 2 charge", or "Lv. 1 of 3 · 1 charge" where more than one
  level is learned, Left and Right moving it). A row the charge or the run refuses is drawn as a row that can't be
  chosen, its icon at 150 alpha. Under the rows, 96 above the panel's foot, the last confirm's line in ExtraBold 30: Red
  for a refusal, Ink for a power put in force. At the right a panel with CHARGE in the Label style, ten pips 34 high (Gold
  for each point held, Rule for the rest, rounded 8) and "4 of 10. A point every 200 steps." in ExtraBold 24 Muted; under
  a rule, IN FORCE and each power in force a line 72 apart (its icon at radius 26, its name and level in Black 28 and the
  minutes left, "9:42", right-aligned), or "Nothing is in force." in Muted.
- **Chips over the field**: while the field is the player's (no script, menu or text over it) each power in force is a
  chip 156 by 48 at the left, 32 in, the first 176 from the top and each next 58 under it: a white pill with a 3-wide
  Frame rim, the kind's icon filling its left end and the minutes left in Black 26 Ink. They stand clear of the pinned
  request and the location sign above them, the Safari Game's count and the run timer at the right, the text box below
  and the autosave disc in the bottom left. A power that wears off is told once on the toast ("Your EXP Power wore off.").

### Badges

- Round medallions in gym order (`ModernUi.Badge`), each in its gym's colour with a simple white mark; our own designs, not the games' badge art. A row holds the region's own count (plan 18 · W2: the names its file lists, none to eight), so Sinnoh's and Kanto's rows show eight and a region with fewer shows fewer; the colours and marks go by the place in the row.
- Not yet won: a flat grey-blue disc with a darker rim, same size, so the row always shows every badge of its region.
- **A region's own badges** (plan 19 · K4): where a badge's name has a look of its own (`Graphics/BadgeLooks.cs`), the medallion takes that colour and that mark instead of the row's; every other badge keeps the row's. Kanto's eight, each a mark of ours that says what the badge is named for and nothing of the games' art:

  | Badge | Colour | Mark |
  | --- | --- | --- |
  | Boulder | stone grey (150, 146, 140) | a squat stone: a rounded block with a smaller block on it |
  | Cascade | water blue (64, 150, 222) | a drop: a disc with a point above it |
  | Thunder | amber (236, 160, 52) | a zigzag bolt of two slanted bars |
  | Rainbow | violet (156, 110, 214) | three rings, one inside another |
  | Soul | rose (222, 92, 140) | a heart of two discs over a point |
  | Marsh | old gold (214, 178, 68) | two discs side by side, overlapping |
  | Volcano | flame red (220, 72, 54) | a flame: a point over a disc |
  | Earth | leaf green (82, 160, 86) | a leaf: a diamond turned on its point |

- **Alola's island stamps** (plan 23 · Z6): a region whose file says `"challenge": "trials"` has stamps, not badges, and the card's caption reads STAMPS. They are the same medallions, in the island guardian's colour with a mark already in the table above: Melemele amber (232, 168, 48) with the bolt, Akala rose (226, 104, 150) with the heart, Ula'ula green (96, 168, 76) with the leaf, Poni blue (70, 132, 210) with the drop.

### Opening and title screen (`TitleScreen`, `TitleScene`)

The order follows the games' opening: a notice, a short film, the legendary Pokémon with the title, "press start", then the menu. Any button skips to the title.

1. **Notice** (3.4 s): white text on black saying this is a fan-made project with original art.
2. **Journey** (3 × 3.6 s): slow pans over the field itself at different times of day (Twinleaf Town in the morning, Route 201 by day, Sandgem Town at night with its windows lit), between cinema bars 120 units tall, dipping to black between shots. They are rendered by the field renderer, so they always match the game.
3. **Reveal** (3.2 s): fade up from black on the void, a white flash, then Giratina and the lettering.
4. **Title**: Giratina (the 3D model, as in battle) hovers in a dark violet void that deepens to crimson, among drifting stones and rising motes. It stays a **shadow** against the glow behind it, picked out by its ink lines; it is never fully lit. Lettering: "Pokémon" in cream over "PLATINUM" in a white-to-silver gradient (`UiFonts.Display`, Nunito Black) with a band of light sweeping across every 5.5 s, a thin rule and "A FAN REMAKE". It is our own lettering, not the games' logo. A pulsing "PRESS Z OR ENTER" sits under Giratina, and a one-line disclaimer at the bottom edge. The build's version stands in the bottom right corner on the disclaimer's line (plan 16 · T2): "v0.1.0" in 20 Bold, in the disclaimer's colour and fading with it, its right edge 48 from the screen's. It is the version alone, never the commit (that goes in the log), so two builds of one version draw the same title; a build made from the source and not from a release's tag reads "v0.0.0-dev".
5. **Notices** (plan 12 · Q1, `TitlePhase.Warning`): what the player must know before choosing comes between "press start" and the menu, as Platinum tells of a damaged save: a notice in the prompt's panel (`ModernUi.Notice`: 1040 by 380, the screen dimmed, rising 60 into place over 0.18 s), its heading in 48 Black, two or three lines in 30 ExtraBold Muted, and one OK button, highlighted, 280 wide at its bottom right; A or B puts it away, and the next notice or the menu follows. A save that couldn't be read says so in our own words: the save before it was loaded (`SaveLoad.Recovered`), or neither could be read and there is no game to continue (`Damaged`, and no CONTINUE card). Plan 16 · T3's word after a crash is a notice of the same kind (`TitleNotice`). It comes first (`TitleNotice.ForCrash`), headed "The game stopped unexpectedly", once, on the start after the crash: it says whether the save is intact (or that no game had been saved yet; a save that couldn't be read is left to its own notice, which follows), names the report (`logs/crash-<date>.txt`) and, where a game was in progress, the copy of it kept beside the save (`savegame.crash.json`). Its body, like any notice's, is at most three lines, which is what the panel holds over its button.
6. **Menu**: the lettering shrinks to the top left, Giratina slides left and cards slide in on the right: a card for each save (plan 12 · Q2, "The slots" below: none with nothing saved), NEW GAME, DELETE A SAVE (only with a save), MIGRATE (only with a save and a file to bring Pokémon from: plan 08 · P21, "The slots" below), HOME (with a save: plan 12 · Q28), BATTLE ROOM (always, since its teams are built from nothing: plan 13 · V13, "The battle room" under "Menu screens"), JUKEBOX, OPTIONS, CREDITS, QUIT. Standard cards.
6. **Menu**: the lettering shrinks to the top left, Giratina slides left and cards slide in on the right: a card for each save (plan 12 · Q2, "The slots" below: none with nothing saved), NEW GAME, DELETE A SAVE (only with a save), MIGRATE (only with a save and a file to bring Pokémon from: plan 08 · P21, "The slots" below), JUKEBOX, GALLERY (only with a save that has seen a Pokémon: plan 12 · Q30), OPTIONS, CREDITS, QUIT. Standard cards.
   - **CONTINUE**, a save alone, shows it at a glance: where it was saved (right of the heading), PLAYER, TIME PLAYED (hours:minutes), POKÉDEX (caught), BADGES n / 8 with the eight medallions (the badges of the region it was saved in, as many as that region has: plan 18 · W2), and the PARTY as 2D icons. The slot it is in is a small tag after the heading.
   - **NEW GAME** with a save present asks first ("Start a new game?"), with No selected by default, and says which slot the new game will be saved in.
   - **NEW GAME+** (plan 13 · V11): where a save has finished a region's story (its Hall of Fame flag) and its boxes can take its team, the question has a third answer: the prompt widens to 1320 for three buttons, NO in Blue, YES in Green and NEW GAME+ in Gold, still opening on NO; up, down, left and right step through the three in their order and round. The blurb names the slot the new game takes, the save it carries from and the region that save finished, and what comes (the Pokédex, the boxes, half the money, a harder journey) in two lines of 30 ExtraBold Muted. Of several finished saves the one played longest is offered. NEW GAME+ opens no Ways to Play: the run is the finished save's a step harder with trainers scaled, and the introduction follows at once. Its Trainer Card's Run page says NEW GAME+ and the count in 40 Black Gold, a little darker, at the right of the run's name where RUN OVER would stand.
7. **Ways to play** (plan 13 · V1, `TitlePhase.ChooseRun`, `ModernUi.RunChoice`): NEW GAME (and, with saves, its YES) opens one panel over the dimmed menu, 1200 wide and 1000 tall, sliding up like the prompts: WAYS TO PLAY in 48 Black over a line in 26 ExtraBold Muted ("Normal is the game as it was made"), then rows 70 apart in the options' pill (`ListRow`, 30 Black; the chosen one a Selection pill, its value right-aligned, the preset's in Selection when not chosen): the preset first (NORMAL, NUZLOCKE, HARDCORE, or CUSTOM once a row has been changed), then Difficulty, Level cap, Trainers scale, Nuzlocke with its three clauses indented 36 under it (only while it is on), Monotype, Party size, No items in battle, No Pokémon Center, No catching, No running, Set battles only, Shiny odds, and START. Nine rows show at once, with a thin track at the right edge when there are more. Under the rows, past a rule, the highlighted row says what it does in 28 ExtraBold Muted (up to three lines), and the key hints stand at the panel's foot (left and right change, A start, B back). Left and right change the row (the preset row steps through the presets); A on the preset row, on START, or anywhere on a run that is still Normal starts the game, so the usual NEW GAME is one more press of A; A on a row of any other run changes it as right does. B goes back to the menu. The choice is kept whole in the save and never changed. **The randomiser** (plan 13 · V6) adds three rows before START: Randomise Pokémon and Randomise items (On or Off; turning either on rolls a seed, turning both off removes it) and, only while either is on, Seed, the nine-digit number the game is made from: left and right roll another, A opens the introduction's keyboard over the dimmed title (the name board with its own heading, SEED, and a line naming the seed now; digits only, a letter passed over, B takes a digit back and then closes it, OK keeps the seed when nothing was typed). A randomised run's card mark reads the preset and the seed (CUSTOM · SEED 20261025), and its Trainer Card page lists the three rows. **Its second half** (plan 13 · V7) puts three more rows of the same kind after Randomise items: Randomise abilities, Randomise moves and Randomise types (On or Off), each turned on alone rolling a seed as the first two do, and the seed going only once all five are off. **Remix** (plan 13 · V15) is one row of the same kind before the randomiser's, after Shiny odds: Remix (On or Off), whose line under the rows says it is a second take on Sinnoh written for this game (the important trainers' teams and the wild Pokémon change; the rules and the story stay); a Remix run is a custom one, so its card mark reads CUSTOM.
8. **Leaving**: 0.8 s fade to black, then the field fades in.

**The loop** (plan 09 · L4): the title left alone for thirty seconds (`TitleScreen.IdleTime`) plays its opening again, as Platinum's title returns to its film: the Journey and the Reveal, never the notice, and back to the title to wait another thirty. It begins in the dip to black every shot begins in, so nothing jumps. A button during it comes to the title with the same flash as the first time, and from there to the menu; the menu, a prompt and a notice wait for as long as it takes.

**The first start** (plan 16 · T24, `FirstStartScreen`): on the game's first run only (no `settings.json` to read), a page comes before the notice: the menu screens' backdrop, BEFORE YOU BEGIN as the screen's title, a line of our own under it in 30 ExtraBold white (that every choice can be changed later under OPTIONS), then the options' own rows (see "Options" under Menu screens: the same cards, pills, arrows and help panel, 78 tall on a pitch of 86, seven at once with the track at the right when there are more), and last CONTINUE, a card of its own with a Green accent and its word centred in 36 Black. Its rows are, where the options have them, the language, the text size, colour vision, high contrast, reduced flashes, reduced motion, captions, the button preset and the graphics quality (with the measured preset once plan 16 · T23 lands); a row a plan hasn't built is left out. A change shows on the page at once (a language, a remap). B does nothing: the page is left by CONTINUE, which keeps the choices and goes on to the notice, and the page never comes again.

**The slots** (plan 12 · Q2): a game is saved in one of three slots, and the menu's column has a card for each slot that holds a save, in the slots' order, above NEW GAME. The column keeps its place (x 1016, 824 wide) and is centred on y 590; with nothing saved it is as it always was (NEW GAME, OPTIONS and QUIT, rows 92 tall on a pitch of 112). With a save its rows are 76 tall, 18 apart, their labels in 36 Black, so that three saves and four rows still clear the disclaimer.
- **One save** is the CONTINUE card above (452 tall), whichever slot it is in.
- **Two or three** are short cards, 144 tall, each a save at a glance: the slot as a caption (SLOT 2, 20 Black Muted) over the player's name (40 Black), 48 from the card's left edge as the rows' labels are, TIME PLAYED beside it (the caption and its value, 300 further right), BADGES n / 8 over the eight medallions at a radius of 13 on a pitch of 28 (504 further right, so the eighth ends at the right margin), and along the card's foot the place it was saved in (24 ExtraBold Muted). No card says whose rules: every game is played by the modern ones (plan 06 · R19). No party and no Pokédex count: those are the lone card's. A press on a card continues that game.
- **A slot that can't be read** (its save and the one kept before it both damaged) is a short card too, so the slot is seen to be taken: its caption, "This save can't be read" in 32 Black and a line under it in 24 ExtraBold Muted. A press on it asks whether to delete it, since nothing else can be done with it.
- **DELETE A SAVE** asks which when there are several: the cursor goes to the cards alone and its glow turns Red, the row reads DELETE WHICH SAVE?, and B goes back. The question then names the slot and whose game it is ("Delete slot 2?", "Lucas in Sandgem Town, 12:34 played. The save and its backup are deleted for good."), with NO, KEEP IT (Blue) first and highlighted and YES, DELETE (Red) after it. The card goes at once; nothing else on the screen moves but the column closing up.
- **NEW GAME** takes the first empty slot and says so in its question ("It will be saved in slot 2. The saves you have are kept as they are."), whose YES, NEW GAME is Green now that nothing is lost by it (Red is the delete question's alone). With all three taken it starts nothing: a notice says a new game needs a slot of its own and that DELETE A SAVE frees one.
- **JUKEBOX** (plan 12 · Q15) is a row between MIGRATE and OPTIONS, always there. It opens the jukebox (see "Jukebox" under Menu screens) over the title and goes back to it with the title's music put back. The column slims its rows by how many it has beside the cards: since CREDITS (plan 16 · T1) six rows are 56 tall on a pitch of 10 and seven (MIGRATE offered too) 48 on a pitch of 8, their labels in 32 Black, which with three saves still clears the disclaimer; with no save the five rows are 92 on a pitch of 20, centred.
- **GALLERY** (plan 12 · Q30) is a row between JUKEBOX and OPTIONS, there only while a save that can be read has seen at least one Pokémon (with nothing seen there is nothing to show, and the title with no save keeps its five rows). It opens the gallery (see "Gallery" under Menu screens) on the species any slot has seen, and goes back to the menu with the cursor on GALLERY. Beside the cards a seventh row is 48 tall on a pitch of 8, as MIGRATE's was, and an eighth (MIGRATE and GALLERY both) 44 on a pitch of 6.
- **CREDITS** (plan 16 · T1) is a row between OPTIONS and QUIT, always there. It opens the title's credits (see "Credits" under Menu screens) over the title, with the title's music going on, and B comes back to the menu with the cursor on CREDITS.
- **An autosave to take up** (plan 12 · Q3): the title's save card of a slot whose game was autosaved after its last save by hand (the file `autosave.json`, left by a game that stopped) asks, before it leaves, in the prompt the delete question uses ("Take up slot 2 from its autosave?"): two buttons, SAVED GAME in Blue and AUTOSAVE in Green with the autosave highlighted, the body naming where the autosave stands and the time played at each. A slot that was only ever autosaved (never saved by hand) has its card as any slot has and leaves into the autosave without asking. B goes back to the menu.
- **MIGRATE** (plan 08 · P21) is a row like OPTIONS, after DELETE A SAVE, and is there only while the `migrate` folder beside the saves holds a file the game can read and at least one save can be read, or while two saves can be read (plan 08 · P22: one to move Pokémon from, one to move them to): a game with no such folder has the title it has always had. It opens the migration's screen (see "Migrate" under Menu screens). With three saves and every row the column keeps its place by slimming its rows (above, under JUKEBOX), so that it still clears the disclaimer.
- **HOME** (plan 12 · Q28, Q29) is a row like OPTIONS, after MIGRATE (or DELETE A SAVE) and before JUKEBOX, there whenever a save can be read. It opens the Home screen (see "Home" under Menu screens). With it the column holds seven rows beside the cards, 48 tall on a pitch of 8, and with MIGRATE too eight, 42 tall on a pitch of 6 with their labels in 32 Black, which with three saves ends above 1012 and so still clears the disclaimer.
- **A battle left on the cards** (plan 12 · Q25): a slot whose battle was left by SUSPEND AND QUIT carries a Gold tag, BATTLE: and whom it is against ("BATTLE: Youngster Tristan", "BATTLE: Starly"), at the foot's right end of its short card (before the run's mark and the packs) and at the party row's right end of the lone card. CONTINUE on it goes straight back into the battle at the menu it was left at, with nothing replayed on screen, and the tag is gone once it has been taken up. One whose game's data has changed since is refused with a notice ("Slot 2's battle can't be taken up") and the slot's own save loads.
- **Notices name their slot**: "Slot 2's last save was damaged" (the one before it was loaded) and "Slot 2 can't be read" (nothing of it could be), one notice to a slot, in the slots' order, after a crash's. After a crash the first line is "Your saves are intact." when there are several and all read whole.
- **Packs on the cards** (plan 16 · T15): a game saved with packs carries a Blue tag, PACK: name for one and "n PACKS" for more, at the foot's right end of its short card (before the run's mark) and at the party row's right end of the lone card. A slot whose game was played with a pack no longer in use, or at another version, has a notice after the slots' own: "Slot n was played with packs", naming each ("gate-lanterns 1.0", "beta 2.0 (now 2.1)") and saying the game goes on without what they changed.

### Motion (`UiMotion`, `UiReveal`)

- Things arrive with an ease-out over 0.2–0.35 s and leave faster with an ease-in (0.1–0.15 s): the start menu and prompts slide, the party's cards rise one after another, the summary's panels slide in from the sides, HP boxes slide in with the Pokémon.
- Bars drain instead of jumping. Selection moves instantly and glows. Nothing bounces except Pokémon icons and the advance arrow.
- Cursors in grids (`UiNav.Grid`) wrap along rows and columns and skip empty slots.
- **Reduce motion** (plan 12 · Q9): with the options' REDUCE MOTION on, every `UiReveal` is at its end the frame it is opened or closed (no slide), and in the field nothing shakes (a scene's shake, a lift's shudder), grass and leaves don't sway (the shaders' `still`; grass still parts round a walker), the picture never ripples (a scene's ripple, the water's waver into a battle) and depth of field is off.

## Areas (targets)

Implemented so far: Twinleaf, Sandgem, Jubilife, Routes 201–202, Lake Verity, Pallet (Kanto's stand-in), and every battle arena (G8: grass and lakeside, forest, cave, water, snow, sand, indoors, a hall per gym type and the League's rooms). Later sessions extend these families; each new area gets a line here.

| Area family | Ground and foliage | Mood |
|---|---|---|
| Towns and routes (Twinleaf, Sandgem, Routes 201–202) | spring greens above, sand paths, teal/red/blue roofs | bright, warm |
| Lakes (Verity, Valor, Acuity) | the same greens, pale stone shores, clear blue water | calm, cooler shade |
| Forests (Eterna) | deeper greens, dark trunks, dappled light | green-tinted shade, stronger vignette |
| Caves and mines | warm browns, cool blue shadows | torch-warm highlights |
| Snow (Route 216–217, Snowpoint) | white-blue ground, dark pines | low sun, cold grading: wherever the player stands in snow country (an area with more snow than lawn), the hour's rig has its sun lowered (its height × 0.7), dimmed and cooled (× 0.74 and `0.92, 0.97, 1.08`), its sky light cooled (`0.94, 0.98, 1.06`), more light bounced up off the ground (× 1.15), its fog cooled toward `0.8, 0.86, 0.96`, and its grading colder: saturation × 0.9, contrast × 1.04, bloom only above 0.95 and half as strong, highlights `0.98, 1.0, 1.06` and shade `0.86, 0.92, 1.18`. It eases in and out over a second at an area's border (`ArtLook.Snowbound`) |
| Coast (Pastoria, Sunyshore) | warm sand, turquoise water | high sun, saturated |
| The Battle Zone (the Fight, Survival and Resort Areas, Routes 224 to 230) | the same greens under broad-leaved round trees, the forest that fills the island round its places included; grey rock in tiers behind the Survival Area and up Route 227 under falling ash, sand under Route 228's sandstorm, turquoise hip roofs on white boards | bright and warm on the coast, drained under ash and sand by the weather's own grading |
| Cities (Jubilife, Veilstone) | pale paving, glass and steel | neutral, crisp |
| Distortion World | grey-violet stone over a deep violet void, rims of pale rock, slabs with a star where the ways are | dim, cool and drained, the same at every hour ("The Distortion World") |

Rooms, caves and the Distortion World have their own rigs that ignore the clock.

## Do and don't

**Do**
- Draw pixel art with placed clusters, one-texel rims and two or three flat shades.
- Keep every pixel-art element on the 32-texel grid and at whole-number scales.
- Use the tokens and components above for every new screen.
- Show before/after boards from the harness for every visual change.

**Don't**
- Don't add random per-texel variation, dithering or "grain" to any texture.
- Don't draw 3D renders of Pokémon in menus, or pixel sprites of Pokémon in battle. (The gallery, plan 12 · Q30, is a scene like the evolution's, not a menu: its model is the one 3D Pokémon on a screen of lists, and its stand-in while the model is built is the still sprite. So are the photo tour's course and a locked heart's opening, plan 44: scenes that show models, never menus.)
- Don't scale pixel art by fractions or filter it bilinearly.
- Don't use near-black outlines, pure black shadows or flat, unshaded UI boxes.
- Don't copy art, models, textures, fonts or UI from the Pokémon games.

## Assets policy (confirmed in G1)

- Procedural generation stays the backbone: it scales to 1025 species and every town.
- Free-licensed assets are welcome where they beat code (OFL fonts, CC0 textures, skies, props), each recorded in `docs/art/CREDITS.md`, and only downloaded with the user's OK.
- An `overrides/models/` folder (ignored by git) lets glTF models replace any species' model (plan 03 · D5, [`docs/model-files.md`](../model-files.md)); `overrides/sprites/` does the same for character sprite frames. What is put there stays on the machine it is put on and is not part of the game's assets.
- Nothing taken from the Pokémon games and no fan rips.

## Known gaps after G11

- The interface is drawn a shape at a time: one or two tenths of a millisecond in the field, 1.2 ms for a double battle's menu. Drawing its shapes in batches would halve that; no scene needs it to stay inside the frame's budget. Battle models are drawn at one level of detail (their triangles are 0.3 ms of a frame), and scenery is batched per chunk rather than instanced, for the same reason: neither is where a frame goes.
- Relief (plan 01 · M3) is drawn from the maps' heights, but no area open so far has any: it is seen on the harness's terrain lab until the hills past Jubilife City open. Raised ground does not shade the ground behind it yet: only its faces cast shadows.
- Snow country has its own grading of the hour's light since plan 01 · M8, caves their own rig since M5, and the Distortion World its own since M8. Its walls and ceilings, walked with sideways gravity in the original, are not built: only its floors are walked.
- Every town of Sinnoh has its buildings and landmarks (plan 01 · M4), each standing where the original's model does; they are seen in the game as plan 01 opens each area, and in the harness's `cities` mode until then. Nine models have plain stand-ins until their area is built, all of them in plan 01 · M10, part b (Stark Mountain's lava and mouth, the Battle Frontier's halls and tower; `docs/world-models.md` says which): part a built the Fight Area's containers and fountains, the Resort Area's pools, the Ribbon Syndicate and the Battleground; the last of M8's (Sunyshore's sea stack, Snowpoint's harbour storehouse and its drifts, Spear Pillar's rifts) were built with the Distortion World. Jubilife City is still the hand-made map in the game itself until plan 01 · M5.
- A cross gable would give the bigger houses their look.
- Puddles have their look and mirror the walker since plan 01 · M7; water mirrors what stands by it and polished floors the walker since plan 09 · L10. A polished floor mirrors only the walker, as the original's do: a pillar of the League's rooms is not seen in it. Only the player's steps leave prints, dust and leaves: other people don't walk about yet (plan 02).
- The weather has its eleven looks, but the five places whose weather follows Platinum's calendar keep one weather until the calendar is imported with them (plan 01 · M7 and M8), and the moods Platinum gives some forests, caves and halls through the same setting are not built. Weather is silent until plan 05. A battle begins in the field's weather (plan 06 · R3) and shows it (plan 25 · Y1: "Weather in battle"), but a thunderstorm, heavy rain and a blizzard come in as plain rain and hail, and the field's ash has no weather in battle, as in the original.
- Rooms are furnished only as far as the maps place furniture; there are no lamps to see, though the light changes at night. Trees are the only field art still built from smooth 3D shapes under a pixel texture.
- The menu screens show what the rules behind them can do so far. The bag uses, gives, tosses and registers every item of Platinum's that works outside battle so far (plan 06 · R11); mail is held but not written, and berries are not planted until berry soil (R14). The PC shows the stored Pokémon thirty to a box in the order they were stored; slots of their own, moving, box names and wallpapers come with R12. The National Pokédex is opened by Professor Rowan in the post-game (plan 02), and the diploma is shown by the Pokédex itself until Jubilife City's Game Freak building is built (plan 01 · M5). The options set the music's and the sound effects' volumes (plan 05 · A1); the cries and the ambience follow the effects' until they have settings of their own (A7).
- Money inside a sentence (a battle's prize) is still written with "$": the mark of the panels is drawn, not typed, so the text renderer has to learn to place it.
- The introduction names only the player. Naming the rival, its last question in Platinum, comes with the story (plan 02 · S4), which has the same name entry to ask with.
- Platinum's thirty-one ways into a battle are all built (plan 09 · L13, L14 and L15). A band's portrait is only as good as the person's rig, which plan 11 · C5 and C6 finish for the Gym Leaders and the League; and the Champion's band takes as long as the Elite Four's, where the original's portraits tremble for nine frames to their thirty-two.
- All 1,025 species have hand-built version 2 models: the whole of Platinum's Sinnoh Pokédex, the 24 the story showed first and the rest of it (plan 03 · D6–D9), and the seven of Sinnoh outside it, so every species of Sinnoh, the 105 of Kanto from outside it, so every species of Kanto, the 64 of Johto from outside it, so every species of Johto, the 107 of Hoenn from outside it, so every species of Hoenn, and all 156 of Unova, so every species of Unova, and all 72 of Kalos, so every species of Kalos, and all 88 of Alola, so every species of Alola, and all 89 of Galar, so every species of Galar, the seven first met in Hisui and all 120 of Paldea, so every species of the National Pokédex (plan 03, decision 3). The generated models (plan 03 · D5), which read as their kind of creature but rarely as the species itself, stood in for each until its sculpt came. Model files in `overrides/models` replace any of them. The forms of generated species are generated too; the 483 forms of hand-built species are hand-built (Platinum's own 64 and the later games' 419), but for Mothim's cloaks, the partner Pikachu and Eevee, Greninja's Battle Bond, Scatterbug's and Spewpa's patterns (which show only once they are Vivillon), the 50% Zygarde with Power Construct, the Own Tempo Rockruff, Minior's meteors (whose cores don't show) the antique Sinistea and Polteageist, the Artisan Poltchageist and the Masterpiece Sinistcha (whose mark is under their foot), which look just like their species and show its sculpt, and the 10% Zygarde with Power Construct, the Gigantamax Appletun and the Low Key Toxtricity's Gigantamax form, which show the 10% Forme's, the Gigantamax Flapple's and the Amped Toxtricity's.
- Characters have mitten hands and no fingers: a point is the whole mitten held out. Every emote can be played on cue by a script since plan 11 · C11 (`act`, with a face held by `mood`), but the story's chapters were written before the commands and none of their scenes uses them yet; the trainer's start and the battle throw are the only ones the game plays by itself.
- The title keeps Giratina in shadow by design; since G7 its model would hold up fully lit if that is ever wanted. The title music is a placeholder melody until plan 05.
- The opening's journey shots are only as good as the maps they fly over; choose new shots as the regions are rebuilt (plan 01, G4–G5).

### The later games' battle places

Plan 37 · FC2. Ours; nothing of X and Y's hosts or halls is recreated.

- **The streak house's rooms** are plan 23's two in Kiloude City as they stand (`KiloudeCity_BattleMaison`, a museum's lobby with the desk and the prize counter in its back wall; `KiloudeCity_BattleMaisonHall`, the hall the challenger and the trainer face each other in, the trainer walking down two tiles from the back wall). The four rooms are one desk's choice and one hall's battles, never four rooms drawn: whoever battles there is dressed in the drawn trainer's look as the Frontier's rooms dress theirs.
- **The four hosts** (`characters.json`'s `house_corvin`, `house_isaura`, `house_tamsin`, `house_ludovic`): grown-ups of the `Adult` build in long coats, each of their room's colour with the same gold trim, so the four read as one house: Corvin of the single room in claret, tall, with swept silver hair; Isaura of the double in teal with long dark hair and a skirt; Tamsin of the triple in violet with her hair up in a bun and a skirt; Ludovic of the rotation in navy with short dark hair and a moustache. Plan 30's grown kit takes them over once it stands.

### Hoenn's last things

Plan 21 · H12, part p. Our own drawings: nothing of Emerald's sprites or metatiles is taken.

- **Birth Island's triangle** (`HoennArt.PaintTriangle`, a card 30 by 32 like any thing of the map): an upright triangle of pale violet glass standing in a dark stone foot, its left face lit and its right in shade, a lit rim up its left edge, a cyan seam down its middle and along its foot meeting in a small glowing core, a glint near the point. Never a heap of stone: it is what Deoxys comes down to.
- **The Abandoned Ship** (`HoennArt.Wreck`, the prop `Wreck` over the hull's tiles in Route 108's sea): the ferry's shape gone to ruin. Its west (stern) half stands half as high above the water as its bow, its hull a stained white with a band of rust at the waterline and rust streaked down from the rail, its portholes and cabin windows dark and empty, its deck of grey weathered planks broken through to the sea in places (more on the sunk stern), its funnel a rusted stump with a dark mouth. Nothing on it is lit after dark.
- **The Mechadolls** (`characters.json`'s `mechadoll`): clockwork dolls, never people: a bald pewter head, an orange band round it, a cyan visor for eyes, a pewter body with brass trim, dark boots, a little under a grown-up's height.
- **The Frontier island's halls and the Trainer Hill** are found at their doors as every GBA building is, but by what the door leads into: the Battle Tower and the Trainer Hill are towers of stone, the six other halls and the Ranking Hall public halls, the Exchange Service Corner a shop, never the cottage a door of no kind is built as.
- **The Trainer Hill's roof** is a room of the airy walkways' look (`InteriorStyle.Aerie`): pale boards under walls of the sky's blue.
- **Marks the story lays** (plan 21 · part r; `Graphics/FieldLife.Marks.cs`): each a block of two cells by two of the life atlas, 32 texels to the tile, drawn with life's pieces. *A plank pressed down*, lying on the deck a tenth of a tile over the height it is walked at: shade (64, 42, 28 at 170) deepest along its north edge where it has dropped, its sides opened into dark gaps, a faint shade over the rest; under the player on one of Hoenn's plank bridges, and under a Kecleon nobody sees on any deck of Hoenn, which is how Route 120's bridge shows something stands there until it is gone. *A heap of salt* in Shoal Cave, standing: a mound of white crystals lit on its left, grey on its right, with glints and a grey-blue rim; once the day's salt is taken *the little left*, a low scatter of crystals on a damp ring; nothing under the tide. *A cave's mouth* the abnormal weather opens in a rock face (the Terra Cave's ways): a dark arch in a rim of rough brown-grey rock, stood at the tile's foot. *Deep water* it lays in the sea (the Marine Cave's ways): one patch to each run of its tiles, deep blue (14, 30, 70 at 150) with soft edges and a few pale flecks.
- **The Abandoned Ship's deck** (`HoennArt.ShipCabin`, overlay props over its blocked tiles): its deckhouse and bulwarks are a ship's steel, never the rock the shared metatiles are laid as: a box from the deck before it to the top of what it covers, a texel wider than its tiles; tall, a white-painted wall streaked with rust over a rusted foot, a band of plate under the roof and dark portholes a tile apart; low, a bulwark under a rusted rail; the roof rusted plate in panels of 16.
- **Blocks of partitions** (the S.S. Tidal's corridor and the walls between its cabins): a run of inner walls with no floor within two tiles that never reaches the edge of the room's walls is the inside of one block and is built as partitions, never shown as a patch of floor; only a run that reaches the edge is the room's dark.
- **A Pokéblock feeder** (plan 21 · H11, part f; `HoennArt.PaintFeeder`, the prop `PokeblockFeeder` the Safari Zone's overlays stand on each feeder's tile): a card 24 by 34, a little roofed box of pale wood on a dark post, a red gable over it, its open front showing a dark tray with a pink Pokéblock left in it, outlined as every card is. It stands in the grass where the feeder is read; the tile is blocked by the import already.
- **A painting on the wall** (plan 21 · H11, part f; `HoennArt.PaintContestPainting`, a `Painting` prop whose model is `contest:<quality>:<colour>`, laid by `GameEngine.HangThePaintings` over each frame's tiles as the gallery is come to): a card 56 by 30, two tiles wide, leaning on the frame's stand where its script is read (the import builds the gallery's frames as low stands, `h3m0_museum_paintings_hung`). A gilt frame, the canvas in its quality's colour (Cool's coral, Beauty's sky, Cute's pink, Smart's leaf, Tough's gold) lit along the top and darker along a floor, the winner as a round body and head in the colour the species data names, two eyes, its shadow, and a white sparkle in the top right. The painting looked at closely stays the Fashion Case's viewer below.

### Hoenn's contests and Pokéblocks

Plan 21 · H11. Our own pictures: nothing of Emerald's stage, judge or Pokéblock sprites is taken.

- **The contest screen** (`ModernUi.Appeal`): the four contestants as cards in the order of their turns, each with its Pokémon's menu sprite on a disc, its name, its stars (a star for each ten of condition), the hearts of the appeal (eight at most, filled as they are won), its points and, when it has the judge's eye, a small eye over the card; a nervous one wears a drop. The crowd is a row of five lights down the side that fill as it is stirred and flash on a burst. The player's four moves are a column of cards at the foot, each with its quality's colour, its hearts and the line of what it does; the lines of the appeals are in the dialogue box.
- **The results** are a board of four rows, each with its condition, its five rounds of hearts and its total, filled round by round, and the winner's row in gold; the award is the ribbon's card on the dialogue box's line.
- **The Pokéblock Case**: a list of up to eight rows, each a Pokéblock (a rounded square in its colour with a lighter top and a glint), its name, its flavour and its feel; a panel of the five flavours beside it. Feeding shows the Pokémon's five qualities before and after as bars.
- **The Berry Blender**: a bowl seen from above with a pocket for each seat, the player's lit; an arm turns, and the pocket under it shows a ring that gives a press's grade (best, good, miss); the speed in the middle in hundredths of a turn a minute; after it the Pokéblock that came out, large on the bowl.
- Colours: the five qualities keep the Super Contest's (Cool red, Beauty blue, Cute pink, Smart green, Tough yellow); a Pokéblock's colour is its name's.
- **The stage behind** (plan 21 · H12, part o; `ModernUi.ContestHallBackdrop`, laid out on `ContestHallStage`'s grid of Emerald's hall, fifteen tiles by eleven): in place of the menus' teal backdrop, the hall the contest is held in, in the quality's colour: curtains in folds along the back under a pelmet, a raised stage of wooden boards with its edge lit, the judge's desk with a lens over it and the host's stand beside it, a ring on the boards where each of the four contestants stands, the audience as plain rounds in their seats, the prize's stand and the painter's easel. Nobody on it is drawn as a person. The whole is dimmed (10, 10, 24 at 120) so the panels read over it. The line-up's four cards stand in a row 380 tall along the foot, over the dialogue box (plan 21 · part r), each with its Pokémon on a disc of 95, so the stage reads above them.
- **A painting** (plan 21 · H11, part b; `ModernUi.Paintings.cs`, on the Fashion Case's viewer): a Master Rank winner of 800 points or more as the painter of the Contest Lobby made it, in the museum's gallery, looked at from before its frame. A gilt frame (a darker gold under a lighter) on the screen's backdrop, the canvas in the colour of its contest lightened (the quality's colour at a quarter, over the cream of the page) with a darker floor under the Pokémon, the Pokémon's menu sprite at five times its size standing on it, the contest's rosette in the canvas's corner, and a dark plaque under the frame with the Pokémon's name, its contest and rank, and its trainer with the points. Either button puts it away.
- **An appeal's burst** (plan 21 · H11, part f; `Models/Contest/AppealBurst.cs`, drawn by `ModernUi.AppealSparks` round the appealer's sprite while a turn plays): 1.2 seconds of eight to fourteen sparks in the move's own quality's colour, shaped by the quality: Cool stars shot out fast and slowing, Beauty glints rising in a swaying column, Cute hearts floating up and out, Smart rings turning round the appealer as they widen, Tough shards flung out and falling. They fade in over the first tenth and out over the last third; where each spark starts is the move's own (its name hashed), so two moves of one quality differ and the same appeal draws the same. Our own, where Emerald plays the move's battle animation on its stage.
